# Second-order optimality, and what it detects

The eigenvalue bound a maximiser must satisfy, the nondegeneracy results that make the component mechanism fire, the $\circ$-orthogonal sum law, and the early bounds on $M(n)$.

Part of [findings/INDEX.md](INDEX.md), the index that says which file to open.

---

### Second-order optimality: a necessary condition on maximizers, and what it does and does not detect

**The criterion.** At a critical point $Q(W)=\theta W$ with $\theta>0$, put $e=W/\theta$, an idempotent of $X\circ Y:=\tfrac12(Q(X+Y)-Q(X)-Q(Y))$, and $L_e=e\circ(\cdot)$. The Hessian of $P$ constrained to the unit sphere of the Weyl space is $6\theta\big(L_e-\tfrac12\mathrm{Id}\big)$, verified through the geodesic second derivative, so

$$\text{$W$ a maximizer}\ \Longrightarrow\ \lambda_{\max}\big(L_e|_{W^\perp}\big)\le\tfrac12 .$$

*The full statement with its argument, scope and attribution is the canonical Lemma L24 at the foot of this file; what is here is the criterion in the context that produced it.*

**Prior art, and it is not new here.** This is **Proposition 6.6** of [Krasnov–Tkachev](../references/krasnov-tkachev-2018-idempotent-variety/notes.md): in any Euclidean metrised algebra an extremal idempotent satisfies $\sigma(L_c|_{c^\perp})\subseteq(-\infty,\tfrac12]$ with $1$ simple. It was derived here independently before the source was found, so this record's version is confirmation rather than discovery.

This is the one constraint on the unknown maximizer that no earlier attack imposed; every previous inequality was first-order. **The hypothesis that cannot be weakened is *maximiser* rather than *critical point*, and the witness is closed-form**: at $S^2\times S^2\subset\mathcal W_4$ the splitting $\mathcal W_4=\mathcal W^+\oplus\mathcal W^-$ is $\circ$-orthogonal, so $Q(W^\pm)=\theta W^\pm$ separately and $Z=W^+-W^-$ is an exact $1$-eigenvector of $L_e$ lying in $W^\perp$, a critical point at which the bound fails by a factor of two. *The sign of $\theta$ separates nothing*: $Q$ is even and $P$ odd, so $e(-W)=e(W)$ and $(-W)^\perp=W^\perp$, making $\lambda_{\max}(L_e\vert_{W^\perp})$ the same number at $\pm\theta$.

**The universality of $\tfrac12$ is NON-GENERICITY, and that is a theorem rather than an observation.** [Krasnov–Tkachev Theorem 3.2](../references/krasnov-tkachev-2018-idempotent-variety/): *a commutative **generic** algebra has $\tfrac12\notin\sigma(A)$*, and conversely $\tfrac12\notin\sigma(A)$ together with the absence of $2$-nilpotents implies genericity, so being generic is essentially equivalent to $\tfrac12$ being absent. Contrapositively, **$\tfrac12\in\sigma(A)$ forces $\mathrm{Weyl}_n$ under $\circ$ to be non-generic**, and the cause is identifiable: $O(n)$-equivariance manufactures orbit directions at every critical point, and those are exact $\tfrac12$-eigenvectors.

**The Euler–Jacobi machinery fails here, and BOTH of its hypotheses fail.** Krasnov–Tkachev's equation (13) reads $\det Df_A(c)=2^{\dim\mathrm{Weyl}_n}\chi_c(\tfrac12)$, *the exponent is the algebra's dimension, and writing it as $2^n$ collides with this record's $n$, which is the base dimension.* So an idempotent is a regular point exactly when $\tfrac12\notin\mathop{\mathrm{spec}}(L_c)$, and $\chi_c(\tfrac12)=0$ always here.

**But "the denominator vanishes" is the wrong diagnosis, and Krasnov–Tkachev say so themselves: the root set is INFINITE.** Euler–Jacobi sums over finitely many simple roots; the $O(n)$-orbit of any idempotent is a positive-dimensional family of idempotents, so the sum has no finitely many terms to take. *Gate 2, reviewer 137.* **And the second hypothesis fails independently of the group:** $\mathrm{Weyl}_n^{\mathbb C}$ contains nonzero **2-nilpotents**, since complex orbits of idempotents are positive-dimensional hence unbounded by the maximum principle, which Krasnov–Tkachev's own compactness argument then rules against $S_{\mathbb C}(\widetilde f)=\{0\}$.

**RETRACTED, the quotient repair.** An earlier reading here held that the degeneracy is *created entirely* by the group action and is therefore what a quotient removes. **Both halves are false.** At $S^3\times S^3$ this record's own datum gives $\dim E(\tfrac12)=18$ against an orbit of dimension $9$: **nine of the eighteen $\tfrac12$-directions are an eigenvalue coincidence with no group origin**, and no quotient touches them. The multiplicity relation is $\mathop{\mathrm{mult}}(\tfrac12)\ge\dim\mathcal O$, an inequality rather than an identity. Worse, **$\circ$ does not descend to $\mathrm{Weyl}_n/O(n)$ at all**, the product of two orbits is undefined, so "syzygies on the quotient" names no object. *No literature supplies an equivariant Euler–Jacobi or Peirce syzygies under a group action; global-residue extensions handle isolated degenerate zeros and never positive-dimensional ones.*

**The attribution of the surviving restrictions was also wrong.** The "valid with some mild restrictions" remark sits in Krasnov–Tkachev §3, not §4.2–4.3, and is attached to the **2-nilpotent** branch; §4.2 concerns **unital** algebras, which $\mathrm{Weyl}_n$ is not. The source calls the branch this record occupies, $\tfrac12\in\sigma$, *"really peculiar"*.

#### Lemma L3, the balanced product is a local maximum in $\mathcal W_n$ at every even $n\ge8$, canonical statement and proof

Needed because the known diagonal-case proof cannot see whether $W^*$'s criticality survives against perturbations that leave the diagonal locus.

*Audited in [ROUND_L03](../verification/ROUND_L03.md): `GAP`, repaired, the repair audited `PROVEN`. Kernel-checked in full at every even $n\ge8$: the Hessian bound `L3_hessian` in [L3Quad.lean](../lean/HamiltonsCubic/Results/L3Quad.lean), its attainment in [L3Attain.lean](../lean/HamiltonsCubic/Results/L3Attain.lean), and the strict local maximum transverse to the orbit, `L3_local_max` in [L3Local.lean](../lean/HamiltonsCubic/Results/L3Local.lean), which moves $V$ to its closest orbit point instead of using the tubular neighbourhood theorem.*

**Statement.** For every **even $n\ge8$**, the unit Weyl tensor $W^*$ of $S^{n/2}\times S^{n/2}$ with $P(W^*)>0$ is a local maximum of $P$ on the unit sphere $\Sigma$ of $\mathcal W_n$, not merely within $\mathcal D_n$, and strictly so transverse to its $O(n)$-orbit $\mathcal O$: some neighbourhood $U$ of $\mathcal O$ in $\Sigma$ has $P<P(W^*)$ on $U\setminus\mathcal O$. The Hessian transverse to $\mathcal O$ is at most $-6\theta/(n-2)$, and the bound is attained.

**Proof.** Write $m=n/2\ge4$ and $\theta=P(W^*)$.

*Criticality and sign.* $W^*$ is critical with $\theta=\theta_{m,m}=\sqrt2\,\frac{m-1}m\sqrt{\frac{n-1}{n-2}}>0$ by [Nienhaus, Proposition 2.25](../references/nienhaus-2023-second-best-einstein/notes.md). The sign is what selects $W^*$ over $-W^*$: $P$ is odd and $e=W/\theta$ is unchanged under $W\mapsto-W$, so the Hessian below changes sign with $\theta$.

*The Hessian.* By [Lemma L24](#lemma-l24-second-order-optimality-as-an-eigenvalue-bound-the-canonical-statement) the Riemannian Hessian at $W^*$ is $6\theta(L_e-\tfrac12\mathrm{Id})$ on $T_{W^*}\Sigma=W^{*\perp}$. [Lemma L27](nondegeneracy.md) at $p=q=m$ gives the values of $L_e$ on $W^{*\perp}$: $\tfrac12$ on the orbit tangent $\mathbb R^m\otimes\mathbb R^m$ and nowhere else, $\lambda_m=\frac{m-2}{2(m-1)}$ on $S^2_0(\mathbb R^m)$ for each factor, $\mu$ on $\Lambda^2\otimes\Lambda^2$ and $-\mu$ on $S^2_0\otimes S^2_0$ with $\tfrac12-\mu=\frac{(m-2)^2-1}{2(m-1)^2}$, so $\mu=\frac1{m-1}$, and $0$ on the remaining four summands. Off the orbit tangent the largest value is $\lambda_m$, since $\lambda_m-\mu=\frac{m-4}{2(m-1)}\ge0$, with a tie at $m=4$. So the Hessian vanishes on $T_{W^*}\mathcal O$, and on its orthogonal complement $N$ in $T_{W^*}\Sigma$
$$\mathrm{Hess}\,P\ \le\ 6\theta\big(\lambda_m-\tfrac12\big)=-\frac{3\theta}{m-1}=-\frac{6\theta}{n-2},$$
with equality on $S^2_0(\mathbb R^m)$.

*From the Hessian to a local maximum.* $\mathcal O$ is a compact submanifold of $\Sigma$, and $P\equiv\theta$ on it by invariance. By the tubular neighbourhood theorem there is $\rho>0$ such that $(x,v)\mapsto\exp_x v$, over $x\in\mathcal O$ and $v$ normal to $\mathcal O$ at $x$ with $\lvert v\rvert<\rho$, is a diffeomorphism onto a neighbourhood of $\mathcal O$. Put $f(v)=P(\exp_{W^*}v)$ for $v\in N$ with $\lvert v\rvert<\rho$. Then $f(0)=\theta$, $df(0)=0$ by criticality, and $d^2f(0)$ is the Hessian on $N$, so Taylor's theorem with a bound $K$ on the third derivative over the closed ball of radius $\rho/2$ gives
$$f(v)\ \le\ \theta-\frac{3\theta}{n-2}\lvert v\rvert^2+K\lvert v\rvert^3 .$$
Each $g\in O(n)$ is an isometry of $\Sigma$ fixing $P$, so $P(\exp_{gW^*}(gv))=P(g\exp_{W^*}v)=f(v)$ and the normal space at $gW^*$ is $gN$: the same bound holds at every point of $\mathcal O$ with the same $K$. For $0<\lvert v\rvert<\rho'=\min\big(\rho/2,\,3\theta/((n-2)K)\big)$ the right side is below $\theta$. So $U=\{\exp_xv:x\in\mathcal O,\ v\perp T_x\mathcal O,\ \lvert v\rvert<\rho'\}$ is a neighbourhood of $\mathcal O$ with $P<\theta$ on $U\setminus\mathcal O$. $\square$

**At $n=6$ the argument stops.** $W^*$ is then $S^3\times S^3$, the pair $(3,3)$ that Lemma L27 excludes: $\mu=\tfrac12$ and $\dim E(\tfrac12)=18$ against an orbit of $9$, leaving nine Hessian null directions transverse to the orbit, so the second-order test decides nothing there. Whether $P$ rises along them is a third-order question, turning on whether $P$ is nonzero on that null space.

So at every even $n\ge12$, the range the conjecture concerns, the balanced product is a strict local maximum transverse to its orbit, and what remains open is whether it is the global one.

#### Lemma L24, second-order optimality as an eigenvalue bound, the canonical statement

*Moved here from [results.md](../results.md) on 2026-08-31. The criterion is stated earlier in this file; what follows is the full statement, argument, scope and attribution, which existed only in that file.*

Needed because every inequality in this programme constrains the unknown maximizer only to first order, through $Q(W)=\theta W$, while a maximizer satisfies more than criticality.

**Statement.** Let $W$ be a critical point with $\theta=P(W)>0$, put $e=W/\theta$, and let $L_e=e\circ(\cdot)$ where $X\circ Y=\tfrac12(Q(X+Y)-Q(X)-Q(Y))$. The **Riemannian** Hessian of $P$ on the unit sphere of $\mathrm{Weyl}_n$, the restriction of the ambient second differential **plus** the constraint's $-3\theta\,\mathrm{Id}$, the two differing by exactly that, is $6\theta\big(L_e-\tfrac12\mathrm{Id}\big)$. Hence if $W$ is a **local** maximiser,

$$\lambda_{\max}\big(L_e|_{W^\perp}\big)\ \le\ \tfrac12 .$$

**And $1$ is a simple eigenvalue of $L_e$**, the bound gives it at once, since every eigenvalue off $W$ is at most $\tfrac12$ while $L_ee=e$. This is the half [trap 48](INDEX-traps.md) asks for and [r306](../reviews/REVIEW_r306.md) K1 uses: it says a maximiser admits no $\circ$-orthogonal splitting **into critical pieces**, because such a splitting would put a second idempotent direction in the $1$-eigenspace. **Criticality of the pieces is the hypothesis and it cannot be dropped**: with $W=\sum_ia_iA_i$ and $e=W/\theta$, the $\circ$-orthogonality gives $e\circ A_j=\frac{a_j}\theta Q(A_j)$, which equals $A_j$ only when $Q(A_j)\propto A_j$. **So this route is strictly weaker than [Lemma L66](support-dichotomy.md)**, which needs no criticality, rather than a second proof of it.

**Argument.** $P$ is a cubic form, so $\nabla P=3Q(W)$ and the second differential is $6\,\theta\,L_e$; the constraint $\|W\|^2=1$ contributes $-3\theta\,\mathrm{Id}$ through its multiplier, leaving $6\theta(L_e-\tfrac12\mathrm{Id})$ on the tangent space. Two steps the first version left implicit. **$L_e$ is self-adjoint**, so $\lambda_{\max}$ is defined at all: $T(X,Y,Z)=\langle X\circ Y,Z\rangle$ is fully symmetric, the $W^2$ part as the symmetrisation of $\mathop{\mathrm{tr}}(XYZ)$ and the $\#$ part because permuting the arguments permutes the slots of both totally antisymmetric $c$'s, sign $(-1)^2$ ([r285](../reviews/REVIEW_r285.md) Claim 1). **And $W^\perp$ is $L_e$-invariant**, since $L_ee=e$ by criticality and self-adjointness then carries the complement, so the restriction is a genuine operator rather than a compression, which is [Lemma L125](#lemma-l125-l24-off-wperp-the-same-statement-in-the-form-the-four-set-application-needs) one heading below.

**Sharpness rests on the orbit directions alone.** $e$ is idempotent, $e\circ e=Q(e)=Q(W)/\theta^2=e$, and the orbit directions are exact $\tfrac12$-eigenvectors by equivariance, attained at every critical point with $\theta\ne0$ because $\mathcal W_n$ carries no $O(n)$-invariant vector, so the orbit is never a point. *Sharpness needs nothing beyond that*: the equivariance identity $2L_W\delta_vW=\theta\,\delta_vW$ with $\delta_vW\perp W$ puts an exact $\tfrac12$-eigenvector in $W^\perp$ at every such point. $\square$ *Machine-checked: the Hessian along great circles as `L24_hessian` in [L24Hess.lean](../lean/HamiltonsCubic/Results/L24Hess.lean), and `L24_sharp` and `L24_lambda_max_eq` in [L24Sharp.lean](../lean/HamiltonsCubic/Results/L24Sharp.lean), with the orbit never a point as `weyl_moves` in [OrbitFree.lean](../lean/HamiltonsCubic/Results/OrbitFree.lean).*

> **SHARPNESS DOES NOT FOLLOW FROM "$\langle e,L_ee\rangle=1$"**: $L_ee=e$ gives $\langle e,L_ee\rangle=\lVert e\rVert^2=\theta^{-2}$, which is $0.6545$ at $n=12$. Repaired to $L_ee=e$ the statement is about the direction $W$ itself and says nothing about $W^\perp$, which is the emptiness [Lemma L125](#lemma-l125-l24-off-wperp-the-same-statement-in-the-form-the-four-set-application-needs) warns of. *Gate 2, [r488](../reviews/REVIEW_r488.md) and [r490](../reviews/REVIEW_r490.md) on identical briefs, agreeing.*

**Scope.** $\theta>0$ carries the division by $6\theta$ that turns $\mathrm{Hess}\le0$ into the eigenvalue bound, and it carries the definition of $e=W/\theta$ itself, **two places, not one**. **At a GLOBAL maximiser it is automatic**, since $P$ is odd and $\max_SP\ge\sqrt{3/2}$ at every $n\ge4$. **At a LOCAL maximiser the record reaches $\theta\ge0$ and no further.** Along the chordal curve through an orbit direction the second- and third-order coefficients vanish unconditionally by [Lemma L51](#lemma-l51-the-variation-calculus-is-an-identity-along-a-critical-orbit-the-canonical-statement), *invoked here at $\theta<0$, outside L51's own stated $\theta>0$, which its proof of these three clauses does not use*, leaving $-\tfrac38\theta\lVert z\rVert^4$ as the leading term; a local maximum needs it $\le0$, hence $\theta\ge0$. **The middle branch is OWED**: nothing on file excludes a local maximiser with $\theta=0$, where $e$ and the whole statement are undefined. *What would close it is that no real $2$-nilpotent of $\mathcal W_n$ is a local maximiser.* **Every consumer applying L24 at a local maximiser inherits that debt**, and the local-maximality contrapositives are where it lands. **The orbit tangent is never zero**, since $\mathcal W_n$ carries no $O(n)$-invariant vector. *The statement that stood here, that such a maximiser satisfies $\lambda_{\min}(L_e\vert_{W^\perp})\ge\tfrac12$, being this theory read at the local minimiser $-W$, is true and vacuous, and is kept only as the reading at $-W$.*

> **THE INEQUALITY DOES NOT REVERSE AT $\mathfrak u(2)\subset\mathfrak{so}(4)$ WITH $\theta=-0.866$.** *Refuted three ways.** $Q$ is even and $P$ odd, so $e(-W)=e(W)$ and $(-W)^\perp=W^\perp$: $\lambda_{\max}(L_e\vert_{W^\perp})$ is *the same number* at $\pm\theta$, and **no witness of any kind can separate the sign**. The label was also wrong, $\mathfrak u(2)$ is $W_{\mathbb{CP}^2}$'s isotropy at $\theta=\pm\sqrt{3/2}=\pm1.2247$, while $0.866$ belongs to $S^2\times S^2$, as [bounds.md](bounds.md)'s own $n=4$ classification records. And the point is a **saddle**: $\mathcal W_4=\mathcal W^+\oplus\mathcal W^-$ is a $\circ$-orthogonal sum, so $\lambda_{\max}(L_e\vert_{W^\perp})=1$ there. *Gate 2, [r488](../reviews/REVIEW_r488.md) and [r490](../reviews/REVIEW_r490.md), independently.* **The claim originates in [Reviewer 27](../reviews/REVIEW_r27.md)** and stood on that audit alone.

**Prior art, this is not new.** The statement is Proposition 6.6 of [Krasnov–Tkachev, *Variety of idempotents in nonassociative algebras*](https://arxiv.org/abs/1801.00617): in any Euclidean metrised algebra, an extremal idempotent satisfies $\sigma(L_c|_{c^\perp})\subseteq(-\infty,\tfrac12]$ with $1$ simple, proven by the same argument, $D^2f(c)\le0$ for $f(x)=\langle x^2,x\rangle/|x|^3$ gives $2L_c-\mathrm{Id}\le P_c$, the projector onto $\mathbb Rc$ (**the paper prints $c\otimes c$, dropping $\lVert c\rVert^{-2}$; see [the notes](../references/krasnov-tkachev-2018-idempotent-variety/notes.md)**), which vanishes on $c^\perp$. It appears again as Proposition 2.5 of [Tkachev's survey](https://arxiv.org/abs/1901.02765). It was derived here independently and before the literature was found, so the derivation is retained above as confirmation; the credit belongs to those authors. Their framework also identifies the whole setting: $\mathcal W_n$ with the cubic $P$ is an algebra $V(u)$ in their sense, its critical points are that algebra's idempotents, and maximizing $P$ is minimizing $\|c\|$ over them.

#### The first variation of $P$ at an arbitrary direction, and the full symmetry of $T$ on self-adjoint operators, the derivation

*Derived 2026-09-19 on Fable, for [I36](../plan.md). Unnumbered until its Lean file lands, which is what the numbering rule asks. The **sketch** of the $\#$ half was already on file, one sentence in [conventions.md](conventions.md)'s $T$ row: "permuting the arguments permutes the slots of two totally antisymmetric $c$'s at sign $(-1)^2$". This is that sentence written out, together with the piece it does not cover, the variation at a direction that is not self-adjoint, which is what Fermat on the full sphere needs. [FoxProduct.lean](../lean/HamiltonsCubic/Base/FoxProduct.lean) records the symmetry as owed, and [L98](../lean/HamiltonsCubic/Results/L98Statement.lean), [L99](../lean/HamiltonsCubic/Results/L99Statement.lean) and [L127](../lean/HamiltonsCubic/Results/L127Statement.lean) each name it as the absence keeping their subject unstatable.*

**Conventions, fixed once.** $\Lambda^2$ is the skew matrices with $\langle A,B\rangle=\tfrac12\mathop{\mathrm{tr}}(A^{\mathsf T}B)$, and $\{e\}$ is any orthonormal basis of it, the $\varphi_{ij}$ at $i<j$. Operators $A,B,\dots$ are linear maps $\Lambda^2\to\Lambda^2$, $A^*$ is the adjoint against $\langle\,,\rangle$, $\mathop{\mathrm{Tr}}$ is the trace on $\mathop{\mathrm{End}}(\Lambda^2)$, so $\mathop{\mathrm{Tr}}M=\sum_e\langle Me,e\rangle$, and the operator pairing is $\langle A,B\rangle_{\mathrm{op}}=\mathop{\mathrm{Tr}}(A^*B)=\sum_e\langle Ae,Be\rangle$. *In the Lean layer's ordered-pair sums, $\sum_e f(e)=\tfrac12\sum_{i,j}f(\varphi_{ij})$, which is the $\tfrac12$ in `innerOp2`.* Matrix entries are $A_{ge}=\langle g,Ae\rangle$, so $Ae=\sum_gA_{ge}\,g$ and $(A^*)_{ge}=A_{eg}$. The structure constants are $c_{efm}=\langle[e,f],m\rangle$.

**Three facts, all on the record.** (i) $\mathop{\mathrm{Tr}}$ is cyclic and $\mathop{\mathrm{Tr}}(M^*)=\mathop{\mathrm{Tr}}M$. (ii) $\mathop{\mathrm{ad}}_X$ is skew-adjoint on $\Lambda^2$ for skew $X$, $\langle[X,U],V\rangle=-\langle U,[X,V]\rangle$, which is `ad_skewAdjoint` in [L33Statement.lean](../lean/HamiltonsCubic/Results/L33Statement.lean). (iii) Hence $c$ is **totally antisymmetric**: antisymmetric in $(e,f)$ from the bracket, and cyclic from (ii), $c_{efm}=\langle[e,f],m\rangle=-\langle f,[e,m]\rangle=\langle[m,e],f\rangle=c_{mef}$.

**Step 1, the cubic form as two traces.** The layer's $\mathrm{sharpQF}(R,\eta)=-\tfrac14\sum_{i,j}\langle[\eta,R[\eta,R\varphi_{ij}]],\varphi_{ij}\rangle=-\tfrac12\sum_e\langle\mathop{\mathrm{ad}}_\eta R\mathop{\mathrm{ad}}_\eta R\,e,e\rangle=-\tfrac12\mathop{\mathrm{Tr}}(\mathop{\mathrm{ad}}_\eta R\mathop{\mathrm{ad}}_\eta R)$, **at every LINEAR $R$, self-adjoint or not.** *A first version wrote "with no hypothesis on $R$" and that is false of the object the layer names* ([r1013](../reviews/REVIEW_r1013.md), [r1014](../reviews/REVIEW_r1014.md), independently): the layer's $R$ is a bare `Matrix → Matrix`, and the right side presupposes it IS an operator. **Witness**: at $n=3$ the constant map to $\varphi_{13}$ with $\eta=\varphi_{12}$ gives $0$ against $-\tfrac12$. *[WeylReal.lean](../lean/HamiltonsCubic/Base/WeylReal.lean) files that same linearity as owed.* Polarising in $\eta$ and using cyclicity for the two cross terms, $\mathrm{sharpBil}(R,X,Y)=-\tfrac12\mathop{\mathrm{Tr}}(\mathop{\mathrm{ad}}_XR\mathop{\mathrm{ad}}_YR)$, and since $R^\#X$ is by definition the vector whose $\varphi$-coordinates are $\mathrm{sharpBil}(R,X,\varphi)$,
$$\langle R^\#X,Y\rangle=-\tfrac12\mathop{\mathrm{Tr}}(\mathop{\mathrm{ad}}_XR\mathop{\mathrm{ad}}_YR)\qquad\text{for all }X,Y\in\Lambda^2 .$$
Then $\langle R,R^\#\rangle_{\mathrm{op}}=\sum_e\langle R^\#e,Re\rangle=-\tfrac12\sum_e\mathop{\mathrm{Tr}}(\mathop{\mathrm{ad}}_eR\mathop{\mathrm{ad}}_{Re}R)$. **Define, for operators $A,B,C$,**
$$\Phi(A;B,C):=\sum_e\mathop{\mathrm{Tr}}\big(\mathop{\mathrm{ad}}_e\,B\,\mathop{\mathrm{ad}}_{Ae}\,C\big),$$
so that $\langle S,R^\#\rangle_{\mathrm{op}}=\sum_e\langle R^\#e,Se\rangle=-\tfrac12\Phi(S;R,R)$ for every $S$, and
$$P(R)=\mathop{\mathrm{Tr}}(R^*R^*R)-\tfrac12\Phi(R;R,R).$$
*The first term is the layer's `trCube2`, $\langle R^2,R\rangle_{\mathrm{op}}=\mathop{\mathrm{Tr}}((R^2)^*R)$, which equals $\mathop{\mathrm{Tr}}R^3$ AT A SELF-ADJOINT $R$.* **"AND NOT OTHERWISE" IS FALSE POINTWISE AND A FIRST VERSION SAID IT** ([r1013](../reviews/REVIEW_r1013.md)): *at a skew-adjoint $R$, $(R^2)^*=R^2$ and $\mathop{\mathrm{Tr}}R^3=-\mathop{\mathrm{Tr}}R^3=0$, so the two sides agree there too.* **The true statement is the quantified one**, that self-adjointness is what makes the identity hold at EVERY $R$. *This is [r1012](../reviews/REVIEW_r1012.md)'s correction carried one step too far, a correction overcorrecting.*

**Step 2, $\Phi$ in indices.** Expanding the trace over a second basis $f$ and moving $\mathop{\mathrm{ad}}_e$ across by (ii), $\Phi(A;B,C)=\sum_{e,f}\langle[e,B[Ae,Cf]],f\rangle=-\sum_{e,f}\langle[Ae,Cf],B^*[e,f]\rangle$. With $Ae=\sum_gA_{ge}g$, $Cf=\sum_hC_{hf}h$, $[g,h]=\sum_kc_{ghk}k$ and $B^*[e,f]=\sum_{m,k}c_{efm}B_{mk}k$,
$$\Phi(A;B,C)=-\sum_{e,f,g,h,k,m}c_{ghk}\,c_{efm}\,A_{ge}\,B_{mk}\,C_{hf}.$$
**This is the whole content**: a contraction of three matrices against two copies of a totally antisymmetric tensor, and every symmetry below is a relabelling of its six summation indices.

**Step 3, the two symmetries of $\Phi$.**
- *Swap the outer slots, no hypothesis.* Rename $g\leftrightarrow h$, $e\leftrightarrow f$: $c_{ghk}c_{efm}\to c_{hgk}c_{fem}=(-c_{ghk})(-c_{efm})$, and $A_{ge}C_{hf}\to A_{hf}C_{ge}$. So $\Phi(A;B,C)=\Phi(C;B,A)$.
- *Rotate, at a symmetric first slot.* Rename $m\to g'$, $k\to e'$ and rotate both $c$'s cyclically by (iii): $c_{ghe'}c_{efg'}=c_{e'gh}c_{g'ef}$, so $\Phi(A;B,C)=-\sum c_{e'gh}c_{g'ef}\,B_{g'e'}\,C_{hf}\,A_{ge}$. Matching this against the definition of $\Phi(B;C,A^{\mathsf T})=-\sum c_{g'h'k'}c_{e'f'm'}B_{g'e'}C_{m'k'}(A^{\mathsf T})_{h'f'}$ at $h'=e,k'=f,f'=g,m'=h$ gives $\Phi(A;B,C)=\Phi(B;C,A^{*})$ **with NO hypothesis**, hence $\Phi(A;B,C)=\Phi(B;C,A)$ **when $A$ is symmetric**, self-adjointness entering only to turn $A^*$ back into $A$. *Both reviewers verified this step, which the brief flagged as likeliest to be wrong, one of them twice by independent routes* ([r1013](../reviews/REVIEW_r1013.md), [r1014](../reviews/REVIEW_r1014.md)); **the adjoint is load-bearing and the rotation fails without it.**

*The two together generate $S_3$ on the slots when all three operators are symmetric, so $\Phi$ is then fully symmetric. That is the sentence in [conventions.md](conventions.md), made checkable.*

**Step 4, the first variation, at every direction.** $P$ is a cubic polynomial on $\mathop{\mathrm{End}}(\Lambda^2)$, so with $U(A,B,C):=\mathop{\mathrm{Tr}}(A^*B^*C)-\tfrac12\Phi(A;B,C)$ one has $P(R)=U(R,R,R)$ and
$$P(R+tS)=P(R)+t\big[U(S,R,R)+U(R,S,R)+U(R,R,S)\big]+t^2\big[U(S,S,R)+U(S,R,S)+U(R,S,S)\big]+t^3P(S),$$
an identity of polynomials in $t$, with no hypothesis. **Now let $R^*=R$ and $S$ be arbitrary.** The trace part of the $t$-coefficient is $\mathop{\mathrm{Tr}}(S^*R^2)+\mathop{\mathrm{Tr}}(RS^*R)+\mathop{\mathrm{Tr}}(R^2S)=3\mathop{\mathrm{Tr}}(S^*R^2)$ by cyclicity and $\mathop{\mathrm{Tr}}(R^2S)=\mathop{\mathrm{Tr}}((R^2S)^*)$. The $\Phi$ part is $\Phi(S;R,R)+\Phi(R;S,R)+\Phi(R;R,S)$, and by step 3, $\Phi(R;R,S)=\Phi(S;R,R)$ with no hypothesis and $\Phi(R;S,R)=\Phi(S;R,R)$ at symmetric $R$, so it is $3\Phi(S;R,R)$. Hence
$$\boxed{\;\frac{d}{dt}\Big|_{t=0}P(R+tS)=3\mathop{\mathrm{Tr}}(S^*R^2)-\tfrac32\Phi(S;R,R)=3\,\langle S,\;R^2+R^\#\rangle_{\mathrm{op}}=3\,\langle S,Q(R)\rangle_{\mathrm{op}}\;}$$
**for every $S\in\mathop{\mathrm{End}}(\Lambda^2)$, at every self-adjoint $R$.** *The direction is NOT required to be self-adjoint, Weyl, or anything else, which is exactly what a Fermat argument over the full unit sphere of $\mathop{\mathrm{End}}(\Lambda^2)$ consumes.* **THE IMPLICATION TO EULER RUNS ONE WAY, AND A FIRST VERSION READ AS THOUGH IT RAN BOTH** ([r1014](../reviews/REVIEW_r1014.md)): *the case $S=R$ gives Euler's $DP(R)[R]=3P(R)$ at the self-adjoint $R$ this identity covers, while Euler itself holds at EVERY $R$ by homogeneity of the cubic and needs none of this.* **What is NOT recoverable from Euler is the general direction**, which is the whole reason the identity is worth having.

**Step 5, full symmetry of $T$.** The layer's $X\circ Y=\tfrac12(Q(X+Y)-Q(X)-Q(Y))=\tfrac12(XY+YX)+X\#Y$, where $\langle(X\#Y)U,V\rangle$ is the polarisation of $\langle R^\#U,V\rangle$ in $R$, so $T(X,Y,Z)=\langle X\circ Y,Z\rangle_{\mathrm{op}}=\tfrac12\mathop{\mathrm{Tr}}(Z^*XY)+\tfrac12\mathop{\mathrm{Tr}}(Z^*YX)-\tfrac14\big[\Phi(Z;X,Y)+\Phi(Z;Y,X)\big]$. **At self-adjoint $X,Y,Z$** the trace half is fully symmetric by cyclicity and $\mathop{\mathrm{Tr}}M=\mathop{\mathrm{Tr}}M^*$, and the $\Phi$ half is fully symmetric by step 3. *So $T$ is fully symmetric on the self-adjoint operators, hence on $\mathcal W_n$, hence $L_e$ is self-adjoint there, which is the debt.* **And $T(X,Y,Z)=\tfrac12[U(X,Y,Z)+U(Y,X,Z)]$ identically**, so the symmetrised $U$ IS $T$ and step 4's coefficient is $3T(R,R,S)$. *It takes TWO facts and a first version credited one* ([r1013](../reviews/REVIEW_r1013.md), [r1014](../reviews/REVIEW_r1014.md)): **the $\Phi$ halves match by the outer-slot swap, and the trace halves by $\mathop{\mathrm{Tr}}M=\mathop{\mathrm{Tr}}M^*$.** *And "hence $L_e$ is self-adjoint there" is about the COMPRESSION to $\mathcal W_n$; self-adjointness as an operator ON $\mathcal W_n$ needs $X\circ Y\in\mathcal W_n$, which is the closing paragraph's own off-file item.*

**What the derivation does not say.** *It is stated on $\mathop{\mathrm{End}}(\Lambda^2)$ and says nothing about whether $Q$ maps $\mathcal W_n$ to itself.* That is a separate classical fact, $\mathop{\mathrm{Ric}}(Q(W))=0$ at Ricci-flat $W$, read off the evolution $(\partial_t-\Delta)R_{ij}=2R_{ipjq}R^{pq}-2R_{ip}R^p{}_j$, whose right side vanishes when $\mathop{\mathrm{Ric}}=0$; it is what turns "critical on the Weyl sphere" into $Q(W)=\theta W$ rather than $\mathop{\mathrm{proj}}_{\mathcal W}Q(W)=\theta W$, and it is not on file.

**Check, exact and by hand.** At $R=\mathrm{id}$, $\Phi(S;\mathrm{id},\mathrm{id})=\sum_e\mathop{\mathrm{Tr}}(\mathop{\mathrm{ad}}_e\mathop{\mathrm{ad}}_{Se})=-\sum_e\langle\mathop{\mathrm{ad}}_e,\mathop{\mathrm{ad}}_{Se}\rangle_{\mathrm{op}}=-2(n-2)\sum_e\langle e,Se\rangle=-2(n-2)\mathop{\mathrm{Tr}}S$ by the Killing contraction `sum_inner2_ad2` (the record's $\sum\langle\mathop{\mathrm{ad}}_X\mathop{\mathrm{ad}}_Y\varphi,\varphi\rangle=2(n-2)\mathop{\mathrm{tr}}(XY)$ read in the present normalisation), so $3\langle S,\mathrm{id}^\#\rangle=-\tfrac32\Phi=3(n-2)\mathop{\mathrm{Tr}}S$, agreeing with $\mathrm{id}^\#=(n-2)\,\mathrm{id}$, the anchor `sharpOp_id`.

#### Lemma L125, L24 off $W^\perp$: the same statement, in the form the four-set application needs

*[r367](../reviews/REVIEW_r367.md) and [r368](../reviews/REVIEW_r368.md), identical briefs, agreeing. **This is not a strengthening of [Lemma L24](#lemma-l24-second-order-optimality-as-an-eigenvalue-bound-the-canonical-statement) and the first version's claim that it was is withdrawn.***

**At a maximiser $W$ of unit norm with $\theta>0$, for every $Y\in\mathcal W_n$,**
$$\langle Y,L_eY\rangle\ \le\ \tfrac12\lVert Y\rVert^2+\tfrac12\langle W,Y\rangle^2 .$$

**Proof, and why it is [Lemma L24](#lemma-l24-second-order-optimality-as-an-eigenvalue-bound-the-canonical-statement).** $L_e$ is self-adjoint with $L_eW=W$, so $W^\perp$ is invariant. Split $Y=aW+Z$ with $Z\perp W$: the cross term $2a\langle Z,L_eW\rangle=2a\langle Z,W\rangle$ vanishes, both sides become $a^2+{}$their $Z$-parts, and the statement collapses to $\langle Z,L_eZ\rangle\le\tfrac12\lVert Z\rVert^2$, **L24, in both directions.** $\square$

> **THE EQUALITY AT $Y=W$ CERTIFIES NOTHING.** It is the radial direction of a degree-zero homogeneous function, identically zero at *any* point, using neither maximality nor criticality. *[DERIVE-mx1.md](../reviews/DERIVE-mx1.md)'s "sharp at $X=W$" inherits the same emptiness.*

**What the form is for.** Applied to [Lemma L121](#lemma-l121-the-4-set-block-its-spectrum-and-one-necessary-condition-per-4-subset)'s four-set block it gives $\sqrt\Sigma\le\theta(1+c^2)$ for the unit maximising direction $\hat X$ of the block's Rayleigh quotient, $c=\langle W,\hat X\rangle$, hence
$$\Sigma\le\theta^2(1+c^2)^2,$$
**which is exactly [DERIVE-mx1.md](../reviews/DERIVE-mx1.md)'s projection-corrected bound.** *Machine-checked: the display as `L125_weyl`, and this step as `L125_fourSet` given L121's block value, in [L125Weyl.lean](../lean/HamiltonsCubic/Results/L125Weyl.lean).* *Corroboration by a second route; the content is L24's and the arrangement is what the four-set application needs.* **And $c$ here is DERIVE-mx1's own $\rho$**, confirmed at Gate 2, not [Lemma L124](#lemma-l124-on-the-half-flat-four-set-family-theta9det-v_-and-theta2letfrac32rho2-with-equality-exactly-at-pm-w_mathbbcp2)'s $\rho$, which is a different quantity.

#### Lemma L233, the pair direction's second-order quotient, and where its $\#$ content actually lives

*Derived 2026-09-23 for route [R33](../routes.md). **The number is the one returned when the earlier L233 was withdrawn on 2026-09-21**, as [INDEX-results.md](INDEX-results.md) records.*

**Statement.** Let $W\in\mathcal W_n$ have unit norm and $\theta=P(W)\ne0$, put $e=W/\theta$ and let $\{\psi_\gamma\}$ be an orthonormal eigenbasis of $e$ on $\Lambda^2$ with $e\psi_\gamma=\varepsilon_\gamma\psi_\gamma$. For any two of them, $u=\psi_\alpha$ and $v=\psi_\beta$ with $\alpha\ne\beta$ **as INDICES** (the eigenvalues may coincide, and at the worked point below they do), set

$$Y_0=\tfrac12\big(uv^{\mathsf T}+vu^{\mathsf T}\big)\in\mathrm{Sym}^2(\Lambda^2),\qquad \lVert Y_0\rVert^2=\tfrac12 .$$

Then $\langle W,Y_0\rangle=0$, **carried by orthonormality rather than by $\alpha\ne\beta$**, and

$$\boxed{\ \frac{\langle Y_0,\,e\circ Y_0\rangle}{\lVert Y_0\rVert^2}\ =\ \tfrac12\big(\langle u,eu\rangle+\langle v,ev\rangle\big)\ -\ \tfrac12\big\langle [u,v],\,e\,[u,v]\big\rangle\ }$$

**THE STATEMENT MENTIONS NO BASIS, AND IT HOLDS AT EVERY SYMMETRIC $e$ ON $\Lambda^2$ AND EVERY ORTHONORMAL PAIR.** *Weyl-ness, criticality, unit norm, $\theta\ne0$ and even the eigenvector hypothesis are all INERT* ([r1083](../reviews/REVIEW_r1083.md), [r1084](../reviews/REVIEW_r1084.md), agreeing, screened at $375$ eigenpairs of random symmetric $E$ at $n=4\ldots7$ and at all $105$ of a random $W\in\mathcal W_6$). **The eigen-form is the special case**: with $e\psi_\gamma=\varepsilon_\gamma\psi_\gamma$ the two halves read $\tfrac12(\varepsilon_\alpha+\varepsilon_\beta)$ and $\tfrac12\sum_\gamma\varepsilon_\gamma\langle\psi_\gamma,[u,v]\rangle^2$, and *the sum there must run over $e$'s OWN eigenbasis*, a real indexing trap both reviewers reproduced independently. **Displaying the quadratic form removes the trap rather than warning about it.**

**The two halves, and which hypothesis each consumes.** The anticommutator half is $\langle Y_0,\tfrac12(eY_0+Y_0e)\rangle=\tfrac14(\varepsilon_\alpha+\varepsilon_\beta)$, which needs only that $u,v$ are orthonormal eigenvectors, since $Y_0^2=\tfrac14(uu^{\mathsf T}+vv^{\mathsf T})$. The $\#$ half is $\langle Y_0,\,e\,\#\,Y_0\rangle=-\tfrac12\sum_\gamma\varepsilon_\gamma\mathop{\mathrm{Tr}}\big((\mathrm{ad}_{\psi_\gamma}Y_0)^2\big)$, and each trace is $\tfrac12\langle\psi_\gamma,[u,v]\rangle^2$ by `trace_sq_pair_direction`, **which needs only that $\mathrm{ad}_{\psi_\gamma}$ is antisymmetric**, neither unit norm nor orthogonality. *The sum runs over $W$'s OWN eigenbasis, and weighting the $\varphi$-basis components of $[u,v]$ by eigenvalues indexed in the eigenbasis is the slip this derivation made first and the screen caught.*

**The $\#$ term is quadratic in the commutator, and THAT is the content.** $\sum_\gamma\langle\psi_\gamma,[u,v]\rangle^2=\lVert[u,v]\rVert^2$, so the $\#$ half vanishes identically whenever $u$ and $v$ commute. **Two disjoint coordinate planes commute**, so on the unprojected pair direction built from them the quotient is the plain average $\tfrac12(\varepsilon_\alpha+\varepsilon_\beta)$ and the $\#$ operation contributes nothing at all.

**ON A COMMUTING PAIR, AND ONLY THERE, THE ADMISSIBLE DIRECTION'S WHOLE $\#$ CONTENT IS CARRIED BY THE PROJECTION ONTO $\mathcal W_n$.** *An earlier version of this sentence said so without the hypothesis, and that general form is REFUTED* ([r1083](../reviews/REVIEW_r1083.md), [r1084](../reviews/REVIEW_r1084.md), agreeing, each with its own counterexample): **at the very configuration below, the pair $\varphi_{01},\varphi_{04}$ SHARES an index, has $\lVert[u,v]\rVert^2=1$ and carries unprojected $\#$ content $+\tfrac1{14}$**, and at a random $W\in\mathcal W_6$ all $105$ eigenpairs have $[u,v]\ne0$. *So the bracket carries $\#$ content at essentially every pair, and the vanishing is the special case.* The commuting case is what [Lemma L26](nondegeneracy.md) has been reporting since 2026-08. **At the $(4,4)$ split at $n=8$, which this record calls $S^4\times S^4$**, for $\pi=\{0,1\}$ in the first block and $\rho=\{4,5\}$ in the second, the unprojected quotient is $\tfrac4{21}$ while $X=\pi_{\mathcal W_n}(\varphi_\pi\odot\varphi_\rho)$ satisfies $e\circ X=\mu X$ with $\mu=\tfrac{n-2}{2(p-1)(q-1)}=\tfrac13$; **the projection moves the quotient by $\tfrac17$, which is the NET of $+\tfrac{16}{63}$ in the $\#$ half and $-\tfrac7{63}$ in the anticommutator half**, and equals $-\varepsilon_{PQ}$ by L26's own three-symbol identity, so *L26 already held both numbers and L233's new content is the general-$W$ closed form.* *L26's closed form is reproduced here independently of its own derivation, from structure constants controlled against [screen_I66_sphere_product_anchor.py](../scripts/misc/screen_I66_sphere_product_anchor.py)'s operator-level `sharpOp`, agreeing to $1.3\cdot10^{-15}$.*

**What this settles for [R33](../routes.md), and what it leaves.** *Settled*: the unprojected formula, at every $W$ and every eigenpair, and the fact that ON A COMMUTING PAIR the projection rather than the bracket carries the $\#$ term. *This clause survived its own repair four lines above and was scoped on 2026-09-23* ([r1085](../reviews/REVIEW_r1085.md)); the unrestricted form is refuted. **The projected quotient is [Lemma L234](#lemma-l234-the-projected-pair-quotient-in-closed-form-at-every-weyl-e)**, the quantity [Lemma L24](#lemma-l24-second-order-optimality-as-an-eigenvalue-bound-the-canonical-statement) and [Lemma L125](#lemma-l125-l24-off-wperp-the-same-statement-in-the-form-the-four-set-application-needs) actually constrain, because $Y_0\notin\mathcal W_n$ and only $\pi_{\mathcal W_n}Y_0$ is an admissible direction. *The unprojected formula is its first term.*

**SUB-STEP (a), THE PROJECTED QUOTIENT AT A TWO-BLOCK SPHERE PRODUCT, IS SETTLED FOR EVERY PAIR OF COORDINATE PLANES BY [LEMMA L27](nondegeneracy.md)'S DECOMPOSITION.** *The projected direction $X=\pi(\varphi_\alpha\odot\varphi_\beta)$ lies in a sum of L27's summands, $L_e$ is a scalar on each, so the quotient is those scalars weighted by the norm shares.* Three cases, by where the two planes sit ($p,q\ge3$), Gate 2 by [r1090](../reviews/REVIEW_r1090.md) and [r1091](../reviews/REVIEW_r1091.md), each rebuilt from the definitions:

| planes | summands reached | quotient |
|---|---|---|
| disjoint, one in each block (L26's hypothesis) | $\Lambda^2\otimes\Lambda^2$ | $\mu=\dfrac{n-2}{2(p-1)(q-1)}$, [Lemma L26](nondegeneracy.md) |
| disjoint, both in the $p$-block ($p\ge4$) | $\mathrm{Weyl}_p$ | $0$, an exact eigenvector |
| sharing one index, both in the $p$-block | $\mathrm{Weyl}_p\oplus S^2_0(\mathbb R^p)$ | $\dfrac{q}{2(p-1)(n-3)}$ |

**The sharing row, derived.** $R=\varphi_{ij}\odot\varphi_{ik}$ has $\varphi_{ij}\wedge\varphi_{ik}=0$, so it is already a curvature tensor, with zero scalar curvature and traceless Ricci $h\propto e_j\odot e_k\in S^2_0(\mathbb R^p)$. In dimension $p$, $R=W_p(R)+\tfrac1{p-2}h\owedge g_P$, and in dimension $n$, $\pi(R)=R-\tfrac1{n-2}h\owedge(g_P+g_Q)$. Subtracting, $\pi(R)=W_p(R)+T$ with $T=h\owedge\bigl(\tfrac{q}{(p-2)(n-2)}g_P-\tfrac1{n-2}g_Q\bigr)$, which is Ricci-free and spans the $S^2_0(\mathbb R^p)$ summand. With $\lVert Y_0\rVert^2=\tfrac12$ the Ricci part costs $\tfrac1{2(p-2)}$ in dimension $p$ and $\tfrac1{2(n-2)}$ in dimension $n$, so $\lVert T\rVert^2=\tfrac{q}{2(p-2)(n-2)}$ against $\lVert\pi(R)\rVert^2=\tfrac{n-3}{2(n-2)}$, and L27's eigenvalues $0$ on $\mathrm{Weyl}_p$ and $\lambda_p=\tfrac{p-2}{2(p-1)}$ on $S^2_0(\mathbb R^p)$ give $\lambda_p\cdot\tfrac{q}{(p-2)(n-3)}=\tfrac{q}{2(p-1)(n-3)}$. $\square$ *It is an eigenvector only at $p=3$, where $\mathrm{Weyl}_3=0$.*

**The sharing value is L26's three-symbol shape times a combinatorial factor**, $\tfrac{q}{2(p-1)(n-3)}=\binom q2\big/\binom{n-2}2\cdot\mu$ ([r1090](../reviews/REVIEW_r1090.md), exact at 18 splits). *Every direction must have that shape at a two-block diagonal $e$: $X=\pi(Y_0)$ does not depend on $e$, so the quotient is linear in $e$, and $\langle X,D\circ X\rangle=\langle Q(X),D\rangle$ with $Q(X)\in\mathrm{Weyl}_n$ annihilates the two-dimensional Ricci part of the three block values, leaving the line $(1,1,-2)$.*

**The bound is [Lemma L27](nondegeneracy.md)'s, and these quotients cannot test it.** L27 already proves $\lambda_{\max}(L_e|_{W^\perp})=\tfrac12$ at every $p,q\ge3$, so no direction at a sphere product exceeds $\tfrac12$, and the table's values sit below it: $0\le\tfrac q{2(p-1)(n-3)}\le\lambda_p<\tfrac12$ and $\mu\le\tfrac12$. *The one place a coordinate pair exceeds $\tfrac12$ is $p=2$, where $\mu$ does, which is the record's $S^2\times S^{n-2}$ saddle.* **The same value holds at the three-factor products** $(3,3,3)$ and $(4,4,4)$ ([r1090](../reviews/REVIEW_r1090.md)), where L27 does not apply, so the formula's scope is wider than its present derivation. *Lean: [I73SharingQuotient.lean](../lean/HamiltonsCubic/Results/I73SharingQuotient.lean) types the closed form, and the decomposition is L27's.*

**Off the sphere products the disjoint quotient has a four-symbol form, screened and not derived**: at every coordinate-diagonal Weyl $e$ and every $n$ it reads $\tfrac12(\varepsilon_{ij}+\varepsilon_{kl})-\tfrac14(\varepsilon_{ik}+\varepsilon_{il}+\varepsilon_{jk}+\varepsilon_{jl})$ with $\lVert X\rVert^2=\tfrac13$ ([r1091](../reviews/REVIEW_r1091.md), exact at $n=6,7,8,10,11$ and 360 random disjoint pairs), and the direction is then generally NOT an eigenvector. *At a sphere product it gives $\mu$ across the blocks and $0$ inside one, the table's first two rows.* **The table covers coordinate-plane pairs only**: $e$'s eigenspaces are degenerate, and random orthonormal pairs inside the $PP$ eigenspace at $S^4\times S^4$ reach $0.291$ against the coordinate value $\tfrac2{15}$ ([r1091](../reviews/REVIEW_r1091.md), a screen).

**SUB-STEP (b), SCREENED: THE PAIR TEST IS SILENT AT BOTH RECORDED OBJECTS, AND AT ONE OF THEM IT IS BLIND TO A KNOWN INSTABILITY.** *For each pair of $e$'s eigenspaces $(E_a,E_b)$, the top eigenvalue of $L_e$ compressed to the projected span of $\{\pi(u\odot v)\}$ with $W$'s component removed bounds every pair quotient from that eigenspace pair from ABOVE, so a compression at most $\tfrac12$ settles that no pair direction fires there.* ([screen_I73_pair_blindness.py](../scripts/misc/screen_I73_pair_blindness.py), both objects validated as Weyl and critical to $10^{-11}$, with $\theta$ matching the record.)

| object | $\theta$ | $\lambda_{\max}(L_e\vert_{W^\perp})$ | max pair compression |
|---|---|---|---|
| padded $W_{\mathbb{CP}^2}$, $n=6$ and $8$ | $\sqrt{3/2}$ | $\tfrac12$ | $\tfrac12$, on the $(-\tfrac13,\tfrac23)$ pair only |
| [post F39](fence-posts.md)'s $n=8$ witness, ten eigenvalues | $1.088948$ | $0.608885$ | $0.492123$ |

**The second row is the finding.** *The witness is a saddle, and every pair inequality [L125](second-order.md) supplies holds there, so the pair inequalities are strictly weaker than second-order optimality: the unstable direction lies outside every eigenspace-pair span.* **That is the first clause of I73's retirement condition met on a stored object**, so R33 can exclude a three-or-more-valued critical point only through the first-order rows of sub-step (c), and never through the pair inequalities alone. *The first row is consistent with padded $W_{\mathbb{CP}^2}$ sitting at the $\theta=\sqrt{3/2}$ boundary with its $\tfrac12$ attained on a two-dimensional span.*
**

**The test is NOT vacuous, by an argument rather than a measurement.** [Post F39](fence-posts.md)'s recurring failure is a direction that turns out to lie in the orbit tangent, where [post F37](fence-posts.md)'s calculus is empty and the quotient is forced to $\tfrac12$. Here $L_e$ is self-adjoint and the orbit tangent is CONTAINED IN its $\tfrac12$-eigenspace, which is all the argument needs, so **any pair direction that is an eigenvector at $\mu\ne\tfrac12$ is orthogonal to the orbit tangent outright**. *Containment and not equality: [Lemma L207](nondegeneracy.md)'s excess figures are exactly the gap, $9$ at the $(3,3)$ split.* **And the clearance covers pair directions that ARE eigenvectors**, which is not every pair direction. At the $(4,4)$ split at $n=8$, which this record calls $S^4\times S^4$, the overlap is $3.7\cdot10^{-16}$ against a $16$-dimensional tangent, which is $\dim\mathfrak{so}(8)-\dim(\mathfrak{so}(4)\oplus\mathfrak{so}(4))$.

**Evidence.** Gate 1 (a) L26's $\mu$ reproduced, (b) the $\#$ operation rebuilt from structure constants and agreeing with the record's independent operator-level definition, (c) the sphere product screened as Weyl, unit norm, $P(W)=\theta_{\mathrm{split}}$ and critical, (d) the closed form checked at $24$ eigenpairs over $6$ generic $W\in\mathcal W_n$ to $2.7\cdot10^{-14}$, with a sign-flipped variant separated by $10.4$ so the check is known able to fail. Script [screen_I73_pair_direction.py](../scripts/misc/screen_I73_pair_direction.py). **Lean**: `trace_sq_pair_direction` in [I73PairDirection.lean](../lean/HamiltonsCubic/Results/I73PairDirection.lean) types and proves the load-bearing trace at every antisymmetric matrix.

#### Lemma L234, the projected pair quotient in closed form, at every Weyl $e$

*Derived 2026-09-23 for [I73](../plan.md)'s sub-step (c), the derivation written out in [the working note](../reviews/2026-09-23-I73c-projected-pair.md). **Gate 2 by [r1093](../reviews/REVIEW_r1093.md) and [r1094](../reviews/REVIEW_r1094.md)** on identical briefs, both CONFIRMING every step and both finding the same GAP in the route conclusion, which is repaired below.*

**Statement.** Let $n\ge5$, $e\in\mathcal W_n$ arbitrary, $u,v\in\Lambda^2$ orthonormal with matrices $U,V$, $Y_0=u\odot v$, $X=\pi_{\mathcal W_n}Y_0$. With $h=\mathop{\mathrm{Ric}}(Y_0)=-\tfrac12(UV+VU)$ and $\omega=(Y_0)_{[ijkl]}$,
$$\langle X,e\circ X\rangle=\langle Y_0,e\circ Y_0\rangle-\frac1{2(n-2)}\sum e_{ijkl}h_{ik}h_{jl}-\frac38\sum e_{ijkl}\,\omega_{ijab}\,\omega_{klab},\qquad \lVert X\rVert^2=\tfrac12-\frac{\lvert h\rvert^2}{n-2}-\lVert\omega\rVert^2_{\mathrm{op}},$$
the first term being [L233](#lemma-l233-the-pair-directions-second-order-quotient-and-where-its--content-actually-lives)'s.

**Proof.** (1) $T$ is fully symmetric on self-adjoint operators and $e\circ\mathcal W_n\subseteq\mathcal W_n$, the polarisation of $Q(\mathcal W_n)\subseteq\mathcal W_n$, which is [Bhattacharya](../references/bhattacharya-2013-curvature-ode/)'s section 3 with $\mathop{\mathrm{Ric}}(Q(R))_{ij}=\sum\mathop{\mathrm{Ric}}(R)_{kl}R_{ikjl}$ (eq. 3.1), after Hamilton. So with $N=Y_0-X\perp\mathcal W_n$, $\langle X,e\circ X\rangle=\langle Y_0,e\circ Y_0\rangle-\langle N,e\circ N\rangle$. (2) $N=h\owedge g/(n-2)+\omega$, and the cross term is an $O(n)$-equivariant map $S^2_0\times\Lambda^4\to\mathcal W_n$ whose only candidate puts three of $W$'s indices into $\omega$, killed by Bianchi. (3) The Weyl part of $Q(k\owedge g)$ is $\tfrac{n-2}2W(k\owedge k)$, read off a diagonal $k$ through the diagonal $R^\#$ formula and extended by equivariance, which gives $\tfrac1{2(n-2)}$. (4) $\mathop{\mathrm{Hom}}(S^2\Lambda^4,\mathcal W_n)^{O(n)}$ is at most one-dimensional by the first fundamental theorem, **the count taken under $O(n)$**, since under $SO(8)$ the tensor $\omega_{ijab}(\star\omega)_{klab}$ is a second generator ([r1093](../reviews/REVIEW_r1093.md)). At $\omega=s\,\mathrm{vol}_B$, $Q(\omega)=3s^2\Pi_B$ against $\sum\omega\omega=2s^2(\delta\delta-\delta\delta)_B$, which gives $\tfrac38$. At $n=5$ both sides vanish. $\square$

**Corollary, the four-symbol law.** For a disjoint coordinate pair $(\varphi_{ab},\varphi_{cd})$ at every Weyl $e$, $n\ge5$, with $e_P=\langle\varphi_P,e\varphi_P\rangle$: the projected quotient is $\tfrac12(e_{ab}+e_{cd})-\tfrac14(e_{ac}+e_{ad}+e_{bc}+e_{bd})$. *[r1091](../reviews/REVIEW_r1091.md)'s screened law, derived, and at every Weyl $e$ rather than the coordinate-diagonal ones it was screened at.*

**What it does to [R33](../routes.md).** *The pair inequality is NON-LOCAL in $(\lambda,T)$, and it is NOT outside the calculus.* At padded $W_{\mathbb{CP}^2}$, $n=8$, the pairs $(\varphi_{45},\varphi_{67})$, $(\varphi_{04},\varphi_{15})$, $(\varphi_{04},\varphi_{25})$ lie in one eigenspace, commute, and share unprojected value $0$ and norm $\tfrac13$, with projected quotients $0,-\tfrac1{12},\tfrac1{24}$. So the quotient is not a function of the pair's own row $(\lambda_\alpha,\lambda_\beta,T_{\alpha\beta\cdot})$, and [r1093](../reviews/REVIEW_r1093.md) exhibits the same across two eigenspaces (quotients $0.5271$ and $0.0288$ at a coordinate-diagonal $e$, a screen). **But the whole squared tensor separates them**: $\sum\lambda_\gamma T_{\gamma\beta\delta}T_{\delta\alpha\varepsilon}$ is $0,\tfrac13,-\tfrac16$ at the three pairs, $-4$ times the quotients ([r1094](../reviews/REVIEW_r1094.md), reproduced here), and $e_{01}$, $e_{02}$ are themselves $\lambda$-weighted $T$-rows of neighbouring pairs. With SIGNED structure constants the quotient is a polynomial in the bracket tensor at every $n\ge5$, $\pi_{\mathcal W_n}$ being a polynomial in the Casimir. **Whether it is a function of the squared $T$ in general is OPEN.** *So sub-step (c) needs row-coupling terms, and a per-pair, row-local inequality does not exist.*

**Lean.** `diag_Q_kg` in [I73ProjectedPair.lean](../lean/HamiltonsCubic/Results/I73ProjectedPair.lean) types step (3)'s diagonal identity, standard three axioms. Step (1)'s closure input is proved: `Qop_isWeylOp` and `foxProd_isWeylOp` in [L209WeylClosure.lean](../lean/HamiltonsCubic/Results/L209WeylClosure.lean). The Ricci half of the projection onto $\mathcal W_n$ is proved as `weylOfBianchi_isWeylOp` in [WeylRicciRemoval.lean](../lean/HamiltonsCubic/Results/WeylRicciRemoval.lean). The full orthogonal projection is proved too, `weylProj_isWeylOp` and `weylProj_orth` in [WeylProjection.lean](../lean/HamiltonsCubic/Results/WeylProjection.lean) and [WeylOrthogonal.lean](../lean/HamiltonsCubic/Results/WeylOrthogonal.lean), and **step (1), the split, is proved** as `L234_split` in [L234Split.lean](../lean/HamiltonsCubic/Results/L234Split.lean). Steps (2)-(4) remain untyped.

#### Lemma L235, the quintic corrections cancel from two combinations of the eigenspace-summed pair inequalities, at three values

*Derived 2026-09-23 at effort `xhigh` for [I73](../plan.md)'s sub-step (c), written out in section 6 of [the summed-pair note](../reviews/2026-09-23-I73c-summed-pairs.md). **Gate 2 by [r1102](../reviews/REVIEW_r1102.md) and [r1103](../reviews/REVIEW_r1103.md)** on identical briefs: every claim CONFIRMED, no witness against any, and the text repairs they found (the symmetry step, the heading's reach, the Lean weight order) applied.*

**Statement.** Let $W$ be a unit maximiser with $e=W/\theta$ having three eigenvalues $\varepsilon_1<\varepsilon_2<\varepsilon_3$, gaps $p=\varepsilon_2-\varepsilon_1$, $q=\varepsilon_3-\varepsilon_2$, and let $S_{ab}\le0$ be the eigenspace-summed [L125](#lemma-l125-l24-off-wperp-the-same-statement-in-the-form-the-four-set-application-needs) pair inequality on $\pi_{\mathcal W_n}(\psi_\alpha\odot\psi_\beta)$, summed over $\alpha\in E_a$, $\beta\in E_b$. Then
$$p\,S_{12}+(p+q)\,S_{13}\le0\qquad\text{and}\qquad q\,S_{23}+(p+q)\,S_{13}\le0$$
are exact inequalities in the class data $(\varepsilon,m,\tau_{abc},G_{12})$: the two quintic moments $\Phi(e;e^2,e^2)$ and $\Omega(e;e^2,e^2)$ of [L234](#lemma-l234-the-projected-pair-quotient-in-closed-form-at-every-weyl-e)'s corrections cancel from both as formal symbols. Every nonnegative combination of $S_{12},S_{13},S_{23}$ with that property is a nonnegative combination of these two.

**Proof.** By L234 the summed corrections are $\Phi(e;P_a,P_b)/(8(n-2))$ and $\tfrac38\Omega(e;P_a,P_b)$, with $F=\Phi,\Omega$ bilinear and symmetric in its last two arguments. At three values $P_a=L_a(e)$ with $L_a$ the Lagrange polynomial, so $F(e;P_a,P_b)=\sum_{i,j}[L_a]_i[L_b]_jF(e;e^i,e^j)$. Five of the six moments reduce to class data ([the note](../reviews/2026-09-23-I73c-summed-pairs.md), section 5), and the sixth enters with the factor $\ell_a\ell_b$, the product of the leading Lagrange coefficients. Here $\ell_1\ell_2=-\tfrac1{p^2q(p+q)}$, $\ell_1\ell_3=\tfrac1{pq(p+q)^2}$ and $\ell_2\ell_3=-\tfrac1{pq^2(p+q)}$, so $w_{12}\ell_1\ell_2+w_{13}\ell_1\ell_3+w_{23}\ell_2\ell_3=0$ exactly when $w_{13}=(p+q)(w_{12}/p+w_{23}/q)$, which is $\tfrac{w_{12}}p(p,p+q,0)+\tfrac{w_{23}}q(0,p+q,q)$ in the order $(w_{12},w_{13},w_{23})$. $\square$

**Why eliminate rather than reduce.** At random Weyl $e$, $n=6,7$, the quintic moments and the combination $Q=M^\Phi_{22}/(8(n-2))+\tfrac38M^\Omega_{22}$ that $S_{ab}$ sees miss every degree-5 class-level invariant by roughly $8$ to $28\%$, a control recovering r1097's cubic reduction exactly ([screen_I73c_quintic.py](../scripts/misc/screen_I73c_quintic.py)). *A screen at generic $e$, saying nothing about three values.*

**Lean.** `lead_signs`, `elim_first`, `elim_second` and `elim_iff` in [I73cElimination.lean](../lean/HamiltonsCubic/Results/I73cElimination.lean), standard three axioms: the signs, the two vanishing combinations and the hyperplane. The bilinear decomposition and the inequalities are stated, not typed, since the layer defines neither $\Phi$ nor $\Omega$.

#### Lemma L236, the gap-weighted eigenpair sum is spectral at every Weyl tensor, and its maximiser corollary is inert above $\sqrt{3/2}$

*Derived 2026-09-23 at effort `xhigh` for [I73](../plan.md)'s sub-step (c), section 7 of [the summed-pair note](../reviews/2026-09-23-I73c-summed-pairs.md). **Gate 2 by [r1104](../reviews/REVIEW_r1104.md) and [r1105](../reviews/REVIEW_r1105.md)**: the identity, the corollary and the Gram elimination CONFIRMED; the corollary's reach, the Fourier–Motzkin paragraph and a catalogue sentence repaired, the last REFUTED as first written.*

**Statement.** For every unit $W\in\mathcal W_n$ with $\theta=P(W)\ne0$ and $e=W/\theta$, with $s_{\alpha\beta}=\langle X,e\circ X\rangle-\tfrac12\lVert X\rVert^2$ at $X=\pi_{\mathcal W_n}(\psi_\alpha\odot\psi_\beta)$ over the eigenbasis of $e$,
$$\sum_{\alpha,\beta}(\varepsilon_\alpha-\varepsilon_\beta)^2s_{\alpha\beta}=\frac{n+4}6\Big[(n-1)\mathop{\mathrm{tr}}e^3-(n-3)\mathop{\mathrm{tr}}e^2\Big].$$
**Corollary.** A unit maximiser satisfies $(n-1)\mathop{\mathrm{tr}}W^3\le(n-3)\theta$, equivalently $2\mathop{\mathrm{tr}}W^3\le(n-3)\langle W,W^\#\rangle$. **It is inert above $\sqrt{3/2}$**: Okumura's bound $\mathop{\mathrm{tr}}W^3\le\frac{N-2}{\sqrt{N(N-1)}}$ ([problem.md](problem.md) item 2) makes it automatic whenever $\theta\ge\sqrt{3/2}$ at $n\ge11$, and a screen says the same at $n=6,7,8$; at $n=5$ it is empty. The maximal torus attains equality at $n=6,8$.

**Proof.** Sum the pieces of $s_{\alpha\beta}$ (norm from the summed-pair note's section 2, unprojected part from its section 4, corrections from [L234](#lemma-l234-the-projected-pair-quotient-in-closed-form-at-every-weyl-e) and section 5) against $(x-y)^2=x^2-2xy+y^2$: only the moments $M_{02}$ and $M_{11}$ appear, and rows (A), (B) of [L104](bounds-relaxations.md#lemma-l104-the-value-type-reduction), with $W^\#\mathrm{Id}=-W$ and the definition of $P$, collapse them; the full computation is the note's section 7. The corollary is [L125](#lemma-l125-l24-off-wperp-the-same-statement-in-the-form-the-four-set-application-needs) summed with nonnegative weights; basis-free, the ordered sum is $\mathop{\mathrm{tr}}_{\mathcal W_n}(\pi\,\mathrm{ad}_e^2\,\pi\,(L_e-\tfrac12))$. $\square$

**What it does to (c) at three values.** Weighted $(p^2,(p+q)^2,q^2)$, this is the combination of [L235](#lemma-l235-the-quintic-corrections-cancel-from-two-combinations-of-the-eigenspace-summed-pair-inequalities-at-three-values)'s two inequalities that cancels the Gram parameter $G_{12}$, whose coefficients there are $-c\,pq$ and $+c\,p^2$ with $c=\frac{n-8}{12(n-2)}$. **So the $G_{12}$-free content of the pair test carries no $\tau$ and says nothing above $\sqrt{3/2}$**, and what the test can still say at three values sits in L235's two inequalities at the ends of $G_{12}$'s interval, which is $\tau$-dependent through $G_{ab}\ge\tfrac12\tau_{ab}$.

**Lean.** `gap_norm_sum`, `gap_sum_collapse`, `gram_free` (ring arithmetic) and `spectral_corollary` in [I73cGapWeighted.lean](../lean/HamiltonsCubic/Results/I73cGapWeighted.lean), standard three axioms. The summed pieces and the Gram coefficients' derivation are stated, not typed.

#### Lemma L237, the Ky Fan bracket cap, and the exact ceiling it puts on L173's ray

*Derived 2026-09-23 at effort `xhigh`, section 9 of [the summed-pair note](../reviews/2026-09-23-I73c-summed-pairs.md). **Gate 2 by [r1109](../reviews/REVIEW_r1109.md) and [r1110](../reviews/REVIEW_r1110.md)**: lemma, constants and corollary CONFIRMED; both derived the exact ray ceiling independently.*

**Statement.** For a unit $X_0$ and orthonormal $X_1,\dots,X_r$ in $\mathfrak{so}(n)$, $\sum_{j}\lVert[X_0,X_j]\rVert^2\le\kappa_r$, the largest sum of $r$ top eigenvalues of $-\mathrm{ad}_X^2$ over unit $X$, and $\kappa_1,\kappa_2=2,4$ for $n\ge4$ ($1,2$ at $n=3$), with $\kappa_4,\kappa_6=6,8$ for $n\ge6$ ($\kappa_4=4$ at $n=4$ and $3+\sqrt5$ at $n=5$). **Corollary.** In class variables, $\sum_br_bP_{ab}\le\kappa_{\sum r_b}$; [L173](bounds-relaxations.md)'s witness violates it, and along L173's spectral ray the rows with one such cap give $\theta\le\tfrac{83\sqrt{21}}{294}\approx1.29372$ exactly, independent of $n$.

**Proof.** Ky Fan's maximum principle, the root-slot spectrum of $-\mathrm{ad}_X^2$ from [L31](stratum-two-negative.md)'s proof (each slot of multiplicity $2$), and the three-slot bound $s^{\mathsf T}Ms\le4$; the ray ceiling from an exact dual certificate on rows (B), (C) of the negative class and the cap $3P_{00}+3P_{02}\le8$. The note's section 9 carries each step. $\square$

**A uniform proof, and $\kappa_r\le r+2$ at every $r$, with $\kappa_{2k}=2k+2$ for $n\ge2k+2$** (derived 2026-09-25; Gate 2 by [r1152](../reviews/REVIEW_r1152.md) and [r1153](../reviews/REVIEW_r1153.md), every item CONFIRMED, both adding the equality at every even $r$). The eigenvalues of $-\mathrm{ad}_X^2$ at $X=\sum_cs_c\varphi_{p_cq_c}$ are $(s_a+s_b)^2$ and $(s_a-s_b)^2$, each twice, per block pair, $s_a^2$ per block and free index, and $0$. Take any $r$ of them, write $a_c=\lvert s_c\rvert$, and on each block pair split the chosen slots into $m$ opposite-sign pairs and $e=\lvert P-M\rvert\le2$ same-sign leftovers. **An opposite-sign pair sums to exactly $2(a_u^2+a_v^2)$**; a leftover is at most $(a_u+a_v)^2$; a block-free slot (block $c$ against a free index) is $a_c^2$. The leftovers form a multigraph $G$ with $E$ edges and multiplicities $m_{uv}\le2$, and the degree-weighted inequality $(a_u+a_v)^2\le(1+d_v/d_u)a_u^2+(1+d_u/d_v)a_v^2$ charges block $u$ the load $d_u+\sum_{e\ni u}d_v/d_u$. **Since $d_v\le(E-d_u)+m_{uv}$ and $\sum_vm_{uv}^2\le2d_u$, that load is at most $E+2$**, so block $u$'s total coefficient is at most (its opposite-sign slots) $+$ (its block-free slots) $+E+2\le r+2$, and the chosen eigenvalues sum to at most $(r+2)\sum_ca_c^2$. *Equality at every even $r=2k$ once $n\ge2k+2$: the doubled $k$-star (centre weight $k$, $k$ leaves of weight $1$, all one sign) has every load exactly $2k+2$, and at $r=6$ the doubled triangle also attains it, already at $n=6$ and $7$. For $r=2,4$ the maximum below $n=r+2$ is smaller ($3+\sqrt5$ at $n=5$, $r=4$), and at odd $r$ the bound is strict. It replaces the enumeration of three-slot configurations.* [L237Cap.lean](../lean/HamiltonsCubic/Results/L237Cap.lean) reduces the cap to exactly this bound on slot sets.

**What it does.** *The caps remove L173's witness point but not its spectrum, so [post F76](fence-posts.md) stands with the witness moved; a sharper cap for the three-plus-three partner profile below $\approx7.724$ would close the ray at $\sqrt{3/2}$.*

**Lean.** All nineteen three-slot configurations, both maximisers among them, and the corollary's arithmetic, in [KyFanBracketCap.lean](../lean/HamiltonsCubic/Results/KyFanBracketCap.lean), standard three axioms. Ky Fan's principle is proved (`kyFan_sum_le`, and `kyFan_eigen` against an explicit eigenbasis, [KyFanPrinciple.lean](../lean/HamiltonsCubic/Results/KyFanPrinciple.lean)) and the block-pair root slots are proved ([RootSlots.lean](../lean/HamiltonsCubic/Results/RootSlots.lean)); the full slot basis at a general normal form, with its kernel slots, orthonormality, completeness and Ky Fan at every skew $X$, is typed under L240 (`slot_kyFan`, `skew_normal_form`, `kyFan_conj`). The cap itself is typed through the uniform proof: `kappa_cap` in [L237SlotCounts.lean](../lean/HamiltonsCubic/Results/L237SlotCounts.lean), $\kappa_r\le r+2$ at every $r$, in the normal form's $\sum_cs_c^2$.

#### Lemma L238, the Gaussian form of a seven-frame's bracket total, and the isotropic bound 42

*Derived 2026-09-23 at effort `high` (`$CLAUDE_EFFORT` read `high`), for the seven-frame lemma owed by section 10 of [the summed-pair note](../reviews/2026-09-23-I73c-summed-pairs.md). **Gate 2 by [r1113](../reviews/REVIEW_r1113.md) and [r1114](../reviews/REVIEW_r1114.md)**: (i) to (iii) CONFIRMED by both, with $i\neq j$, $d\ge5$ and the $d=5$ value added, and the claim that both known $42$-frames are isotropic REFUTED by the pencil frame.*

**Setting.** $X_1,\dots,X_7$ orthonormal in $\mathfrak{so}(n)$ under $\langle X,Y\rangle=-\tfrac12\mathop{\mathrm{tr}}XY$; $Q_i=-X_i^2$, $P=\sum_iQ_i$ (so $\mathop{\mathrm{tr}}P=14$), $S_{ij}=X_iX_j+X_jX_i$, $d=\mathop{\mathrm{rank}}P$; $v$ a standard Gaussian vector and $G(v)$ the $7\times7$ Gram matrix of $X_1v,\dots,X_7v$; $T=\sum_{i\neq j}\lVert[X_i,X_j]\rVert^2$, the ordered total. A seven-frame does not fit in $\mathfrak{so}(4)$, whose dimension is $6$, so $d\ge5$.

**Statement.** (i) For each pair $i\neq j$, $\lVert[X_i,X_j]\rVert^2=2\mathop{\mathrm{tr}}(Q_iQ_j)-\tfrac12\lVert S_{ij}\rVert_F^2=\mathbb E\,\lvert X_iv\wedge X_jv\rvert^2-4$, hence
$$T=\mathbb E\big[(\mathop{\mathrm{tr}}G)^2-\lVert G\rVert_F^2\big]-168 .$$
(ii) With $r=\min(7,d-1)$, $T\le(1-\tfrac1r)\big(196+2\mathop{\mathrm{tr}}P^2\big)-168$. (iii) **When $P$ is a multiple of the identity on its range, $T\le42$**: the bound of (ii) is $336/d$ for $d\ge8$ and $37.8,\ 41.07,\ 42$ at $d=5,6,7$. It is attained at $d=8$ by the octonion frame $L_{e_i}/2$ ($P=\tfrac74 I_8$) and at $d=7$ by the complement of $\mathfrak g_2$ in $\mathfrak{so}(7)$ ($P=2I_7$ by $G_2$-irreducibility of $\mathbb R^7$).

**Proof.** (i) For skew $X,Y$, $\lVert[X,Y]\rVert^2=\mathop{\mathrm{tr}}(X^2Y^2)-\mathop{\mathrm{tr}}(XYXY)$ and $\mathop{\mathrm{tr}}S^2=2\mathop{\mathrm{tr}}(XYXY)+2\mathop{\mathrm{tr}}(X^2Y^2)$ by cyclicity, which is the first form. Isserlis' fourth moment $\mathbb E[(v^{\mathsf T}Av)(v^{\mathsf T}Bv)]=\mathop{\mathrm{tr}}A\mathop{\mathrm{tr}}B+2\mathop{\mathrm{tr}}AB$ for symmetric $A,B$ gives $\mathbb E[G_{ii}G_{jj}]=4+2\mathop{\mathrm{tr}}Q_iQ_j$ and, for $i\ne j$, $G_{ij}=-\tfrac12v^{\mathsf T}S_{ij}v$ with $\mathop{\mathrm{tr}}S_{ij}=-4\langle X_i,X_j\rangle=0$, so $\mathbb E G_{ij}^2=\tfrac12\lVert S_{ij}\rVert_F^2$. Summing over $i\neq j$ gives the displayed identity. (ii) Each $X_i$ vanishes on $\ker P$ and maps into $\mathop{\mathrm{ran}}P$, so restrict to $W=\mathop{\mathrm{ran}}P\cong\mathbb R^d$ with $v$ Gaussian there. $X_iv\perp v$ puts the seven vectors in a $(d-1)$-space, so $G(v)$ has rank at most $r$, and Cauchy–Schwarz on its eigenvalues gives $(\mathop{\mathrm{tr}}G)^2-\lVert G\rVert_F^2\le(1-\tfrac1r)(\mathop{\mathrm{tr}}G)^2=(1-\tfrac1r)(v^{\mathsf T}Pv)^2$. Isserlis again gives $\mathbb E(v^{\mathsf T}Pv)^2=196+2\mathop{\mathrm{tr}}P^2$. (iii) $\mathop{\mathrm{tr}}P^2=196/d$ in the isotropic case, and the arithmetic is case by case. $\square$

**(ii) at $r=7$ needs no Gaussian.** With $M_{ij}=\mathop{\mathrm{sym}}(X_i^{\mathsf T}X_j)-\delta_{ij}P/k$ for a family of $k$ skew matrices, the per-pair identity and $\sum_iQ_i=P$ give the exact identity $$\sum_{i\neq j}\lVert[X_i,X_j]\rVert^2=2\big(1-\tfrac1k\big)\mathop{\mathrm{tr}}P^2-2\sum_{i,j}\lVert M_{ij}\rVert_F^2,$$ orthonormality unused. It is the Gaussian identity with $\mathbb E\lVert G-\tfrac{\mathop{\mathrm{tr}}G}7I\rVert_F^2$ evaluated, so it proves (ii) at $r=7$ and (iii) for $d\ge8$ outright, and it names the deficiency: equality holds exactly when the $X_i$ pairwise anticommute and every $Q_i=P/k$. The Gaussian is needed only for the rank refinement at $d\le7$.

**What it does.** *It splits the seven-frame lemma.* The isotropic class is closed at $42$, sharp at the octonion and $\mathfrak g_2$-complement frames. **The remainder, anisotropic $P$, contains a frame at $42$ as well**, the pencil frame $X_i=u\wedge w_i$ (all through one vector $u$, distinct from section 10's star-realised frames), where step (ii) loses: it has $T=42$, $\mathop{\mathrm{tr}}P^2=56$, and (ii) returns $96$, because $G(v)=xx^{\mathsf T}+t^2I$ has one eigenvalue far from the rest. **Closing the lemma needs a pointwise bound on $(\mathop{\mathrm{tr}}G)^2-\lVert G\rVert_F^2$ that sees the spread of $G$'s spectrum**, or a reduction of an arbitrary frame to an isotropic one that does not lower $T$. *Literature: the DDVV-type bound for skew matrices ([Ge 2014](../references/ge-2014-ddvv-skew-symmetric/notes.md), constant $\tfrac23$) gives $65.3$ for a seven-frame, being sharp at three matrices; the Clifford-span theorem of Ge–Li–Zhou ([survey, Thm 3.10](../references/ge-li-tang-zhou-2024-ddvv-survey/README.md)) gives $42$ only inside a Clifford span; the Lu–Wenzel row bounds ([notes](../references/ge-li-lu-zhou-2019-lu-wenzel-conjectures/notes.md)) are conjectures, and in this norm they give a row at most $14$ and a top-six sum at most $16$, weaker than $\kappa_6=8$. None bounds a seven-frame below $54.064$.* **The lemma holds in any orthogonal representation $\rho$ of $\mathfrak{so}(n)$**, with $\rho(X_i)$ renormalised by the index $\iota_\rho$ ($-\tfrac12\mathop{\mathrm{tr}}\rho(X)\rho(Y)=\iota_\rho\langle X,Y\rangle$) and $T$ replaced by $T/\iota_\rho$. In the spin representation, $\iota=N/8$, the pencil frame becomes isotropic and its bound is exactly $42$. *Screened: on ascent-maximised frames at $n=8$ the smaller of the vector and spin bounds still reaches $56.6$, so the two representations together do not close the lemma.*

**Lean.** `frame_identity` and `frame_bound` type the Gaussian-free identity and (ii) at $r=7$ whole, for every $k$ and $n$. `bracket_quad` (the trace form of (i)'s first equality), `sigma2_le` (the pointwise step) and `isotropic_frame_bound` with `isotropic_frame_d7`, `isotropic_frame_d8`, in [FrameGaussianBound.lean](../lean/HamiltonsCubic/Results/FrameGaussianBound.lean). Isserlis' moment and the rank bound enter `isotropic_frame_bound` as hypotheses, `hM` being (ii) with $\mathop{\mathrm{tr}}P^2=196/d$ already substituted, so (ii) at $r=d-1<7$ for general $P$ is untyped. The equality case and the sharp frames' own $T=42$ are prose. *Lean fidelity of the identity: [r1115](../reviews/REVIEW_r1115.md), CONFIRMED.*


#### Lemma L239, the seven-frame lemma on the triangle side

*Derived 2026-09-24 at effort `medium`, in [the stability note](../reviews/2026-09-24-I73-seven-frame-stability.md), after a [targeted sweep](../reviews/2026-09-24-LIT-I73-sweep.md) found no published bound. **Gate 2 by [r1119](../reviews/REVIEW_r1119.md) and [r1120](../reviews/REVIEW_r1120.md)**: every item CONFIRMED, the constant corrected from $53.04$ to $53.05$, the top-eigenspace hypothesis made explicit, the scope widened.*

**Statement.** Let $X_0,\dots,X_6$ be orthonormal in $\mathfrak{so}(n)$, $n\ge6$, $\langle X,Y\rangle=-\tfrac12\mathop{\mathrm{tr}}XY$, and suppose some $X_i$ has row deficit $\delta_i=8-\sum_{j\ne i}\lVert[X_i,X_j]\rVert^2<0.277$ and a TRIANGLE top eigenspace: the six largest eigenvalues of $-\mathrm{ad}_{X_i}^2$ are the three $+$ slots of three of its planes. Then $T=\sum_{i\ne j}\lVert[X_i,X_j]\rVert^2\le53.05$. **In particular, a frame with $T>54.064$ has no triangle-type element**, since [L237](#lemma-l237-the-ky-fan-bracket-cap-and-the-exact-ceiling-it-puts-on-l173s-ray) forces its least deficit below $0.277$.

**Proof.** Split each partner as $X_j=Y_j+D_j$ with $Y_j$ in the top eigenspace $E=\mathfrak m_J$, the anti-$J$-linear part of $\mathfrak{so}(W)$, and $\eta=\sum\lVert D_j\rVert^2$. Ky Fan against $E$ gives $g\eta\le\delta_i-y$, $y$ the angle deficit and $g$ the spectral gap; the gap lemma gives $\tfrac12g+y\ge0.277$, so $\eta\le\tfrac12$. With $T=2\,\mathrm{row}_i+T'$, the partners' own total $T'$ splits into the $YY$ part (energy of $E$ at most $24$), the $YD+DY$ part (orthogonal to the first by weights, and at most $4\sum Q(D_j)\le9.16\eta$ with $Q=2,8,2,0$ on $\mathfrak{su}(3)$, $\mathbb RJ$, $W\wedge W^\perp$, $\mathfrak{so}(W^\perp)$), and the $DD$ part ($\le\tfrac43\eta^2$, Ge's skew DDVV). The note carries each step. $\square$

**What it does.** *It closes half of the split the note makes: the seven-frame lemma owed by [I73](../plan.md) now needs only frames all of whose near-extremal elements are star-type, where the kernel slot of the dominant plane costs $4Q/g=20.6$ and the note's estimate returns about $63$.* **The neighbourhood is settled too**: the fifty-two other three-slot configurations (plus, minus and kernel slots) are each at most $3.733$ times the angles' squared norm for nonnegative angles, the exact maximum $2+\sqrt3$ being attained by the path with a kernel slot and by the star with one minus slot, so every element of deficit below $0.466$ has a triangle or a star on top. **So a frame with $T>54.064$ has every such element star-type, and the star side is all that is owed.**

**Lean.** The gap lemma, `triangle_gap_other`, `triangle_gap_same`, `triangle_gap_small` and their quadratic cores, and the fifty-two configuration ceilings `slot_ceiling_*` with `slot_threshold`, in [SevenFrameGap.lean](../lean/HamiltonsCubic/Results/SevenFrameGap.lean), standard three axioms. The slot spectrum, the reduction and the assembly are prose.

#### Lemma L240, the seven-frame lemma: every orthonormal seven-frame in so(n) has bracket total at most 54.064

*Derived 2026-09-24 at effort `medium`, in [the stability note](../reviews/2026-09-24-I73-seven-frame-stability.md). **Gate 2**: the triangle side is [L239](#lemma-l239-the-seven-frame-lemma-on-the-triangle-side) ([r1119](../reviews/REVIEW_r1119.md), [r1120](../reviews/REVIEW_r1120.md)); the star side by [r1121](../reviews/REVIEW_r1121.md) and [r1122](../reviews/REVIEW_r1122.md), who REFUTED its count as first written (the kernel leak charged unnormalised, and the plateau's ceilings untyped with a class missing), and the repair by [r1123](../reviews/REVIEW_r1123.md), CONFIRMED with margin $0.0106$.*

**Statement.** For $X_0,\dots,X_6$ orthonormal in $\mathfrak{so}(n)$, any $n$, with $\langle X,Y\rangle=-\tfrac12\mathop{\mathrm{tr}}XY$: $T=\sum_{i\ne j}\lVert[X_i,X_j]\rVert^2\le54.064$.

**Proof.** $T=56-\sum_i\delta_i$ with $\delta_i=8-\mathrm{row}_i\ge0$ ([L237](#lemma-l237-the-ky-fan-bracket-cap-and-the-exact-ceiling-it-puts-on-l173s-ray)), so if $T>54.064$ some element has $\delta<1.936/7=0.2765714\ldots$ (typed as `exists_small_deficit`). Every three-slot configuration other than the triangle and the star is at most $3.733$ times the angles' squared norm (fifty-eight configurations, repeated kernel loops included, `slot_ceiling_*`), so that element's top eigenspace is a triangle or a star. **A triangle** gives $T\le53.05$ ([L239](#lemma-l239-the-seven-frame-lemma-on-the-triangle-side)). **A star**: split each partner into its part in the top space $E$, a kernel-type leak $K_j$ (maps from the dominant plane out of the plane and the leaves, which moves a partner only at second order, since its Gram with $E$'s images vanishes) and a first-order leak $R_j$; row $0$ pays $g_K\sum\lVert K_j\rVert^2+g_M\sum\lVert R_j\rVert^2\le x-y$ with the star gaps $0.677\,g_M+y\ge0.3$, $3\,g_K+y\ge0.3$; each partner sits at distance $\rho(\kappa_j)-\lVert R_j\rVert$ from the triangles and stars, $\rho(\kappa)^2=\tfrac7{12}-\tfrac\kappa3-\tfrac14\sqrt{1-\kappa^2}$ with $\kappa$ normalised, and pays $\min(0.534,6d^2(1-d^2/4))$ in its own row; the damage is at most $\tfrac12\lVert K_j\rVert^2+2.4\lVert R_j\rVert^2$, and the count $\sum_{j\ge1}\delta_j\ge3.204-5.416x>1.936-x$ for $x<0.2871$ contradicts $T>54.064$. The note carries each step. $\square$

**What it does.** *It discharges the seven-frame lemma [I73](../plan.md) owed: by section 10 of [the summed-pair note](../reviews/2026-09-23-I73c-summed-pairs.md), a seven-frame cap $T_7\le54.064$ added to the capped three-value system puts [L173](bounds-relaxations.md)'s ray ceiling below $\sqrt{3/2}$.* **The best frames known total $42$**, so the lemma is not sharp.

**Lean.** The gap lemmas, the fifty-eight configuration ceilings, the deficit identities and the normalised leak constants, in [SevenFrameGap.lean](../lean/HamiltonsCubic/Results/SevenFrameGap.lean), standard three axioms. **Prose**: the slot spectrum, the Ky Fan weights, the Gram computation, von Neumann's inequality for skew matrices, and the final count.
#### Lemma L243, the Gaussian subspace cap at every dimension, in class form

*Gate 2 by [r1126](../reviews/REVIEW_r1126.md) and [r1127](../reviews/REVIEW_r1127.md), independently: items 1 to 5 and the Lean statements CONFIRMED by both, the scope sentence PLAUSIBLE and repaired below. **The cap is proved in Lean at the frame level for $r=n-1$**, `L243_frame` in [GaussianRankStep.lean](../lean/HamiltonsCubic/Results/GaussianRankStep.lean), and for $r=k$ it is L238's `frame_bound` ($2(1-\tfrac1k)\mathop{\mathrm{tr}}P^2$, the same right side); and the $\min(k,n-1)$ statement is `L243_min`, assembled from both ([r1130](../reviews/REVIEW_r1130.md), [r1131](../reviews/REVIEW_r1131.md) named the gap), with the Gaussian form ([GaussianPairForm.lean](../lean/HamiltonsCubic/Results/GaussianPairForm.lean)) and the rank step both proved; the class-form arithmetic is in [L243GaussianSubspaceCap.lean](../lean/HamiltonsCubic/Results/L243GaussianSubspaceCap.lean). Untyped: the reading of the class variables.*

**Statement.** Let $X_1,\dots,X_k$ be orthonormal in $\mathfrak{so}(n)$, any $k\ge1$, with $P=\sum_i(-X_i^2)$ and $T=\sum_{i\ne j}\lVert[X_i,X_j]\rVert^2$. Then
$$T\ \le\ \Big(1-\frac1r\Big)\big(4k^2+2\mathop{\mathrm{tr}}P^2\big)-4k(k-1),\qquad r=\min(k,\,n-1).$$
**In class form**, for $V$ the sum of the eigenspaces in a set $S$ of classes, $k_S=\sum_{a\in S}m_a$:
$$\sum_{a,b\in S}\sum_c\tau_{abc}\ \le\ \Big(1-\frac1{r_S}\Big)\Big(4k_S^2+2\sum_{a,b\in S}G_{ab}\Big)-4k_S(k_S-1),\qquad r_S=\min(k_S,n-1),$$
**a constraint linear in the class values** $(\tau,G)$.

**Proof.** L238's proof uses $k=7$ nowhere. (i) gives $T=\mathbb E[(\mathop{\mathrm{tr}}G)^2-\lVert G\rVert_F^2]-4k(k-1)$ with $G(v)$ the Gram of $X_1v,\dots,X_kv$, since each ordered pair contributes $\mathbb E\lvert X_iv\wedge X_jv\rvert^2-4$. The $k$ vectors lie in $v^\perp$, so $\mathop{\mathrm{rank}}G\le\min(k,n-1)$, and Cauchy–Schwarz on $G$'s eigenvalues with Isserlis' $\mathbb E(v^{\mathsf T}Pv)^2=(\mathop{\mathrm{tr}}P)^2+2\mathop{\mathrm{tr}}P^2$, $\mathop{\mathrm{tr}}P=2k$, gives the bound. In class form $P=\sum_{a\in S}A_a$, so $\mathop{\mathrm{tr}}P^2=\sum_{a,b\in S}G_{ab}$, and $T$ is the full bracket total over $V$, $\sum_{a,b\in S}\sum_c\tau_{abc}$. $\square$

**Checks.** *Tight on the whole algebra at every $n\ge3$*: $k=\binom n2$, $P=(n-1)I$, and both sides are $n(n-1)(n-2)$, $1320$ at $n=12$. **It excludes [L242](bounds-relaxations.md#lemma-l242-the-capped-three-value-class-system-reaches-above-sqrt32-under-every-valid-seven-frame-cap)'s point**, which was already unrealisable: $S=\{0,2\}$, $k=24$, $\sum G=288$, left side $464$ (its $c=1$ terms $\tau_{001},\tau_{012},\tau_{122}$ are zero), right side $\tfrac{28800}{11}-2208\approx410.2$; *and again at $S=\{1\}$, $824$ against $\approx770.2$* ([r1127](../reviews/REVIEW_r1127.md)). [L241](bounds-relaxations.md#lemma-l241-off-l173s-ray-the-capped-three-value-class-system-still-reaches-above-sqrt32-with-an-exact-witness) satisfies it ($196=140+56$, the $56$ from its $c=1$ terms, $\le\approx264.7$).

**What it does to (c), on a screen.** Added for all six proper class unions to the $n=12$ screen with section 5 and $T_7\le54.064$: **234 of 2080 triples stay feasible at $\theta=1.2248$**, against 237 without it ([screen_I73c_capped_offray.py](../scripts/misc/screen_I73c_capped_offray.py) `12 10 54064/1000 sec5 gauss`). *It excludes L242's point ($\theta\approx1.361$) and keeps L241's ($\theta\approx1.336$). On a screen at $\theta=1.2361$, just above $\theta_0(12)$, the live threshold at $n=12$ ([trap W7](INDEX-traps.md)), 190 of 2080 triples survive with it and every other constraint.* **Owed**: the cap for frames drawn partly from a class, whose $\mathbb E\mathop{\mathrm{tr}}P^2$ needs the per-element $\mathop{\mathrm{tr}}(\Psi\Psi^{\mathsf T})^2\le2$ bound.


#### Lemma L244, the per-vector ceiling on a symmetric three-value critical point

*Gate 2 by [r1128](../reviews/REVIEW_r1128.md) and [r1129](../reviews/REVIEW_r1129.md), independently: every item and the Lean statements CONFIRMED; the labels, the checks sentence and a survivor count repaired below. Both items are kernel-checked: (i) at every unit vector of every eigenspace is `L244_i_unit`, with $b_{ab}$ read at an eigenframe whose class starts at $x$ (frame free at one class per eigenvalue, by hand, [r1883](../reviews/REVIEW_r1883.md)), and (ii) as stated is `L244_ii` in [L244Kappa.lean](../lean/HamiltonsCubic/Results/L244Kappa.lean), with $\kappa_r(x)$ defined by Ky Fan's maximum principle and identified with the top-$r$ eigenvalue sum of $-\mathrm{ad}_x^2$, and $a=1/\sqrt{2p}$ derived from tracelessness and the unit norm in [L244Norm.lean](../lean/HamiltonsCubic/Results/L244Norm.lean); (ii)'s $x$-free bound $\theta\le a(n-5)$ at an eigenvalue $-a$ with $W\preceq a$, $a>0$, is `L244_xfree_op` in [L248Op.lean](../lean/HamiltonsCubic/Results/L248Op.lean), using neither the three-value spectrum nor the multiplicities. Working note: [the per-vector rows](../reviews/2026-09-25-I73c-per-vector-rows.md).*

**Setting.** $W$ a unit critical point, $W^2+W^\#=\theta W$, with three eigenvalues; $P_a$ the eigenprojections, $B_{ab}=P_a\#P_b\succeq0$, and for unit $x$, $b_{ab}(x)=\langle B_{ab}x,x\rangle=\tfrac12\sum_{\gamma\in E_a,\delta\in E_b}\langle[\psi_\gamma,\psi_\delta],x\rangle^2$. $\kappa_r(x)$ is the sum of the top $r$ eigenvalues of $-\mathrm{ad}_x^2$.

**Statement.** (i) **The per-vector rows.** Since $Q(\mathcal W_n)\subseteq\mathcal W_n$ ([L234](#lemma-l234-the-projected-pair-quotient-in-closed-form-at-every-weyl-e), step (1)), $W^\#=\theta W-W^2$ is an operator identity, so for every unit $x\in E_c$:
$$\sum_{a,b}b_{ab}(x)=n-2,\qquad\sum_{a,b}\lambda_b\,b_{ab}(x)=-\lambda_c,\qquad\sum_{a,b}\lambda_a\lambda_b\,b_{ab}(x)=\lambda_c(\theta-\lambda_c),$$
with source caps $2\sum_bb_{ab}(x)\le\kappa_{m_a}(x)$, and $\kappa_{m_c-1}(x)$ for $a=c$ since $x\in\ker\mathrm{ad}_x$. (ii) **The symmetric family.** If the eigenvalues are $(-a,0,a)$, $a=1/\sqrt{2p}$, with multiplicities $(p,N-2p,p)$, **the indices $0,1,2$ label the eigenvalues $-a,0,a$ in that order, so $E_0$ is the NEGATIVE eigenspace and $E_1$ the kernel of $W$**; then every unit $x\in E_0$ gives
$$\theta\ \le\ \frac{\min\big(\kappa_{p-1}(x),\ \kappa_p(x)-2,\ n-4\big)-1}{\sqrt{2p}},\qquad\text{in particular}\qquad\theta\le\frac{n-5}{\sqrt{2p}} .$$

*The family's value $a=1/\sqrt{2p}$ is the unit norm $2pa^2=1$. **Whether the family has a member at any $n$ is open**: the record exhibits none, a hand screen found none at $n=4$, and if it is empty at every $n$ then (ii) is vacuous ([r1856](../reviews/REVIEW_r1856.md) O1, O6). The question is [I114](../plan.md)'s, the symmetric family being that blocked item.*

**Proof.** (i) Pair $\sum_{a,b}B_{ab}=I\#I=(n-2)I$, $\sum_{a,b}\lambda_bB_{ab}=I\#W=-W$ (row (B), a Weyl identity) and $\sum_{a,b}\lambda_a\lambda_bB_{ab}=W^\#=\theta W-W^2$ with $x$; each right side is $(\cdot)\,x$ on $E_c$. The caps: $\sum_{\gamma\in E_a}\lVert[x,\psi_\gamma]\rVert^2=2\sum_bb_{ab}(x)$ by invariance, and Ky Fan bounds the trace of $-\mathrm{ad}_x^2$ over the $m_a$-dimensional $E_a$, or over $E_c\cap x^\perp$ for $a=c$. (ii) At $x\in E_0$, row (B) divided by $a$ is $b_{12}+b_{22}-b_{00}-b_{01}=1$, and row (C) divided by $a^2$ is $2b_{02}-b_{00}-b_{22}=K$ with $K=\theta/a+1$. Row (A) then gives $K=(n-4)-4b_{00}-4b_{01}-b_{11}\le n-4$; the $E_0$ cap gives $K\le2b_{02}\le2(b_{00}+b_{01}+b_{02})\le\kappa_{p-1}(x)$; the $E_2$ cap gives $K+2\le2b_{02}+2(b_{12}+b_{22})\le\kappa_p(x)$. $\square$
