**Checks.** *The operator identity and the per-vector rows hold at $S^5\times S^7$ to $10^{-16}$ at random eigenspace vectors, and the per-vector ceiling there is tight*, its smallest value over random vectors of the extreme classes being $S^5\times S^7$'s own $\theta=1.2282$ (a screen of the general six-variable programme, [screen_I73c_per_vector_ceiling.py](../scripts/misc/screen_I73c_per_vector_ceiling.py)); both reviewers reproduced it independently. *$S^5\times S^7$, with multiplicities $(35,21,10)$, is not in the symmetric family; what (ii) solves in closed form is the same programme at the symmetric spectrum, whose screened $p=12$ values $5/\sqrt{24}$ and $6/\sqrt{24}$ it reproduces.*

**What it does to (c) at $n=12$.** The $x$-free bound $\theta\le7/\sqrt{2p}$ excludes every symmetric three-value critical point above $\theta_0(12)$ with $p\ge17$ ($\tfrac{49}{34}<\tfrac{220}{144}$), and not at $p=16$, which [L250](#lemma-l250-at-n12-the-symmetric-family-has-no-critical-point-above-theta_0-with-p16) excludes by another route. **Below that, an equal-angle $x\in E_0$ caps $\theta$ at $5/\sqrt{24}\approx1.0206$ and a rank-four equal-angle one at $6/\sqrt{24}\approx1.2247$ at $p=12$**, both below $\theta_0(12)$, so the negative eigenspace of such a point contains neither. *[L242](bounds-relaxations.md#lemma-l242-the-capped-three-value-class-system-reaches-above-sqrt32-under-every-valid-seven-frame-cap)'s spectrum is consistent with the $x$-free bound ($1.361\le1.4289$).* **Owed**: the same derivation at a general three-value spectrum, where the asymmetric survivors live: 179 of the 190 at $\theta=1.2361$ under L240's seven-frame constant, 138 of 147 under the sharp $T_7\le42$ (screens).


#### Lemma L248, the operator ceiling: $\lvert\lambda_{\min}\rvert(\theta+\lvert\lambda_{\min}\rvert+2\lambda_{\max})\le(n-2)\lambda_{\max}^2$ at every critical point

*Derived 2026-09-25 in [the per-vector note](../reviews/2026-09-25-I73c-per-vector-rows.md), generalising the $x$-free half of [L244](#lemma-l244-the-per-vector-ceiling-on-a-symmetric-three-value-critical-point) (ii) to every spectrum. Typed and proved in [L248OperatorCeiling.lean](../lean/HamiltonsCubic/Results/L248OperatorCeiling.lean) with the per-vector rows as hypotheses, and from the operator itself as `L248_op` in [L248Op.lean](../lean/HamiltonsCubic/Results/L248Op.lean): $(W-M)\#(W-M)\succeq0$ in $W-M$'s eigenframe, expanded by criticality, $I\#W=-W$ and $I\#I=(n-2)I$ ([r1772](../reviews/REVIEW_r1772.md), which also found the ceiling exactly tight at $W_{\mathbb{CP}^2}$, $\lambda=-2$, $M=4$). **Gate 2**: [r1178](../reviews/REVIEW_r1178.md), [r1179](../reviews/REVIEW_r1179.md), independently, every item CONFIRMED (both rebuilt $\#$ on $\mathfrak{so}(12)$ and saw the $S^5\times S^7$ gap equal $\lambda_{\min}\big((W-b)\#(W-b)\big)=0.1886$); the ceiling itself found in no source searched.*

**Statement.** Let $W$ be a unit critical point, $W^2+W^\#=\theta W$, with eigenvalues $\lambda_{\min}=-a<0<b=\lambda_{\max}$ (any number of distinct values). Then for every eigenvalue $\lambda_c$,
$$\lambda_c\,(\theta-\lambda_c+2b)+(n-2)\,b^2\ \ge\ 0,\qquad\text{in particular}\qquad a\,(\theta+a+2b)\ \le\ (n-2)\,b^2 .$$
At $a=b$ this is L244's $\theta\le a(n-5)$.

**Proof.** $W^\#=\theta W-W^2$, $I\#W=-W$ and $I\#I=(n-2)I$ give $(W-b)\#(W-b)=\theta W-W^2+2bW+(n-2)b^2I$. In the classes of the spectrum the left side is $\sum_{a,b}(\lambda_a-b)(\lambda_b-b)B_{ab}$, every coefficient a product of two nonpositive numbers and every $B_{ab}=P_a\#P_b\succeq0$, so it is positive semidefinite. Pairing with a unit eigenvector of $\lambda_c$ gives the first inequality, and $\lambda_c=-a$ the second. $\square$ In L244's variables it is rows (A), (B), (C) with multipliers $b^2,-2b,1$ (`operator_ceiling`, over any number of classes; `symmetric_case` recovers L244; `witness` shows the hypotheses satisfiable).

**Checks.** *At $S^5\times S^7$, normalised, $(\lambda_{\min},\lambda_{\max})=(-\rho/11,7\rho/44)$ with $\rho=\theta=1.2282$: $0.193\le0.382$.*

**What it does.** A closed-form ceiling on $\theta$ from the two extreme eigenvalues alone, at every critical point. **It adds no exclusion to a class-level screen**: traced over $E_{\min}$ it is the traced rows with multipliers $b^2,-2b,1$ plus a nonnegative combination of the traces $t_{abc}$, which every screen carrying the rows and trace nonnegativity already imposes ([r1179](../reviews/REVIEW_r1179.md)). At $\lambda_1=0$ on three values it is the ceiling behind [F78](fence-posts.md) and `slice_needs_rho_gt_one`.

#### Lemma L249, the per-vector ceiling through the smallest eigenvalues of $-\mathrm{ad}_x^2$

*Derived 2026-09-25 in [the per-vector note](../reviews/2026-09-25-I73c-per-vector-rows.md). **Gate 2**: [r1186](../reviews/REVIEW_r1186.md), [r1187](../reviews/REVIEW_r1187.md), independently, the chain CONFIRMED, the star scope corrected. The linear reduction is typed in [L249SpectralCeiling.lean](../lean/HamiltonsCubic/Results/L249SpectralCeiling.lean) with the spectral step as the hypothesis `hμ`.*

**Statement.** On the symmetric family ([L244](#lemma-l244-the-per-vector-ceiling-on-a-symmetric-three-value-critical-point)'s setting, $m=\dim E_1$), every unit $x\in E_0$ gives
$$\kappa\ \le\ n-5-\tfrac12\mu_m(x)\ =\ \tfrac12\kappa_{2p}(x)-3,$$
with $\mu_m(x)$ the sum of the $m$ smallest eigenvalues of $-\mathrm{ad}_x^2$ on $\mathfrak{so}(n)$ and $\kappa_{2p}(x)$ the sum of the $2p$ largest.

**Proof.** With $h=E_0\oplus E_1$ and $A=\mathrm{ad}_x|_h$, $\lVert(P_0+P_1)A\rVert_F^2=2(b_{00}+2b_{01}+b_{11})$ by ad-invariance and $\mathop{\mathrm{rank}}P_2A\le p$, so Eckart–Young gives $\sum_{i>p}\sigma_i(A)^2\le2(b_{00}+2b_{01}+b_{11})$; $h$ has codimension $p$, so interlacing gives $\sigma_{p+j}(A)\ge\sigma_{2p+j}(\mathrm{ad}_x)$ and the left side is at least $\mu_m(x)$. L244's rows give $b_{00}+2b_{01}+b_{11}\le4b_{00}+4b_{01}+b_{11}=n-5-\kappa$. Row (A) as a trace is $\mathop{\mathrm{tr}}(-\mathrm{ad}_x^2)=2(n-2)$, whence the $\kappa_{2p}$ form. $\square$

**What it does.** It dominates L244's $\kappa_p(x)-3$ and $n-5$ terms. **At $n=12$, $p\le16$, it is strict at every $E_0$ except those whose every element has $\dim\mathfrak c(x)\ge m$**, and those are excluded by a centraliser count ([r1184](../reviews/REVIEW_r1184.md), [r1185](../reviews/REVIEW_r1185.md)): L244's $x$-free ceiling is never attained there. **At a star with $p\le n-3$ it gives $\kappa\le p-3$, the value [F77](fence-posts.md)'s witness attains**, so it is tight there and does not close the window.

#### Lemma L250, at $n=12$ the symmetric family has no critical point above $\theta_0$ with $p=16$

*Derived 2026-09-25 in [the per-vector note](../reviews/2026-09-25-I73c-per-vector-rows.md). **Gate 2**: [r1191](../reviews/REVIEW_r1191.md), [r1192](../reviews/REVIEW_r1192.md), independently, all seven items CONFIRMED. Arithmetic typed in [L249SpectralCeiling.lean](../lean/HamiltonsCubic/Results/L249SpectralCeiling.lean) (`p16_below_theta0`, `label_count`, `p16_near_dec_contradiction`).*

**Statement.** On the symmetric family at $n=12$ ([L244](#lemma-l244-the-per-vector-ceiling-on-a-symmetric-three-value-critical-point)'s setting), every critical point with $p=\dim E_0=16$ has $\theta<\theta_0(12)$. With L244's $p\ge17$, a symmetric three-value critical point above $\theta_0(12)$ has $p\le15$.

**Proof.** If some unit $x\in E_0$ has $\mu_{34}(x)\ge1/60$, [L249](#lemma-l249-the-per-vector-ceiling-through-the-smallest-eigenvalues-of-mathrmad_x2) gives $\kappa\le7-\tfrac1{120}$ and $\theta^2\le703921/460800<220/144$. Otherwise, by [r1189](../reviews/REVIEW_r1189.md)'s characterisation each unit $x\in E_0$ has $s_2\le2/\sqrt{60}\approx0.2582$ or $s_1-s_6\le2/\sqrt{60}$; these closed sets are disjoint and cover the connected unit sphere of $E_0$, so one is all of it. Not the second: its elements are invertible, while $x\mapsto xe_1$ maps $E_0$ into an 11-dimensional space. So $s_2\le2/\sqrt{60}$ throughout, whence $T=\sum_{i\ge2}s_i^2\le\tfrac54\mu_{34}<\tfrac1{48}$ by a count of the fourteen smallest labels, and $\mathop{\mathrm{tr}}x^4\ge2(1-\tfrac1{48})^2$ on the unit sphere. The sphere average $\mathbb E\mathop{\mathrm{tr}}x^4\le3\mathop{\mathrm{tr}}A_0^2/288$ then forces $\mathop{\mathrm{tr}}A_0^2\ge184.08$, against $\mathop{\mathrm{tr}}A_0^2\le\tfrac{11}2\mathop{\mathrm{tr}}A_0=176$, since $A_0\preceq\tfrac{11}2I$: $A_1=11I-2A_0\succeq0$ from $A_0=A_2$ and $\sum_cA_c=11I$ (Ricci-flatness and the Casimir identity; [L246](#lemma-l246-the-isotropy-of-two-eigenspaces-together-is-capped-by-the-directions-where-it-is-small) at $r=1$ gives the same cap), as [r1191](../reviews/REVIEW_r1191.md) notes. $\square$

**What it does.** It closes the case L244 left open at $n=12$ (`p16_not_excluded`). *At $p=15$ the edge $7/\sqrt{30}$ sits $0.04$ above $\theta_0(12)$ and L249 needs $\mu_{36}\ge0.46$, outside the range of the two-type characterisation used here; [L251](#lemma-l251-at-n12-the-symmetric-family-has-no-critical-point-above-theta_0-with-p15) closes it with a sharper average and a dichotomy proved at that range.*

#### Lemma L251, at $n=12$ the symmetric family has no critical point above $\theta_0$ with $p=15$

*Derived 2026-09-26 in [the per-vector note](../reviews/2026-09-25-I73c-per-vector-rows.md). **Gate 2**: [r1198](../reviews/REVIEW_r1198.md), [r1199](../reviews/REVIEW_r1199.md), independently, every item CONFIRMED, five wording repairs applied. Typed in [L251P15.lean](../lean/HamiltonsCubic/Results/L251P15.lean): `quartic_sum_le` from `wedge_gram`, and every scalar step.*

**Statement.** On the symmetric family at $n=12$ ([L244](#lemma-l244-the-per-vector-ceiling-on-a-symmetric-three-value-critical-point)'s setting), every critical point with $p=15$ has $\theta<\theta_0(12)$. With L244 and [L250](#lemma-l250-at-n12-the-symmetric-family-has-no-critical-point-above-theta_0-with-p16), a symmetric three-value critical point above $\theta_0(12)$ has $p\le14$.

**Proof.** Suppose $\theta\ge\theta_0(12)$. Then $\kappa=\theta\sqrt{30}\ge6.7700$ and [L249](#lemma-l249-the-per-vector-ceiling-through-the-smallest-eigenvalues-of-mathrmad_x2) gives $\mu_{36}(x)<0.46$ at every unit $x\in E_0$. In angles $s_1\ge\dots\ge s_6\ge0$, $\sum s_i^2=1$, $u=s_1^2$, $\mu_{36}=2T_{15}$ with $T_{15}$ the sum of the 15 smallest of the thirty values $(s_i\mp s_j)^2$, so $T_{15}<0.23$ throughout.

- *Fourth moment.* Over the unit sphere of $E_0$, $\mathbb E\mathop{\mathrm{tr}}x^4=(2\mathop{\mathrm{tr}}A_0^2+S)/(p(p+2))$ with $S=\sum_{a,b}\mathop{\mathrm{tr}}(\psi_a\psi_b\psi_a\psi_b)$, and `wedge_gram` summed over $a,b$ gives $2p-S=\lVert\sum_a\psi_a\wedge\psi_a\rVert^2\ge0$. With $\mathop{\mathrm{tr}}A_0^2\le\tfrac{11}2\mathop{\mathrm{tr}}A_0=11p$ ($A_0\preceq\tfrac{11}2I$, as in L250): $\mathbb E\mathop{\mathrm{tr}}x^4\le24/(p+2)=24/17$.
- *Gram row sums.* Any $q$ of the values $(s_a\pm s_b)^2$ on a vertex set carrying $\sum s^2=T$ sum to at most $(q+1)T$ (Gershgorin on the Gram matrix of the vectors $e_a\pm e_b$). All twenty on five vertices sum to $8T$, all twelve on four to $6T$.
- *The slice $0.83\le u<0.885$ holds no such $x$*: every value at vertex 1 exceeds $(\sqrt u-\sqrt{1-u})^2\ge0.2487$, so the 15 lie on vertices $2..6$ and sum to at least $2(1-u)>0.23$.
- *A singular such $x$ has $u\ge0.885$.* For $u\le0.23$ every $s_i^2\ge0.08$ ($i\le5$), the sums $(s_i+s_j)^2$ exceed $0.23$, and five values $s_i^2$ give $T_{15}\ge0.4$. For $0.46<u<0.885$ vertex 1 enters only through gaps $d_{1j}$; none gives $2(1-u)>0.23$, and one forces $d_{1j}+s_j^2\ge u/2>0.23$ to exclude $s_j^2$ twice and the three $(s_j+s_k)^2$, which caps the gaps at one and leaves $T_{15}\ge\min(\tfrac{12}{17},\tfrac{12}{19})u>0.29$. For $0.23<u\le0.46$, $s_2^2\ge0.135$ and $Q=s_3^2+s_4^2+s_5^2\ge0.08$, and splitting the 15 by how many touch $\{1,2\}$ gives $T_{15}\ge\min(3Q,\,2s_2^2,\,0.33,\,0.49)>0.23$ (`vertex_split`, `three_way`, `four_way`, `band_constants`); the note carries each case.
- *Connectivity.* $x\mapsto xe_1$ maps the 15-dimensional $E_0$ into $e_1^\perp$, so $E_0$ holds a singular unit $x_0$ with $u(x_0)\ge0.885$. The continuous $u$ takes an interval of values on the connected unit sphere, missing $[0.83,0.885)$, so $u\ge0.885$ throughout and $\mathop{\mathrm{tr}}x^4\ge2u^2\ge1.566>24/17$. $\square$

**What it does.** It closes $p=15$, the last value L244 and L250 left open at $n=12$ on the symmetric family, so there $p\le14$. *The average $24/(p+2)$ replaces L250's $33/(p+2)$ because $S\le2p$ where L250 used $S\le\mathop{\mathrm{tr}}A_0^2$, which is at least $4p$.* **At $p=14$ this average does not close** ($\mu_{38}\le0.92$ allows $\mathop{\mathrm{tr}}x^4$ near $1.465$ against $1.5$, screen), and [L252](#lemma-l252-the-coupled-fourth-moment-and-at-n12-no-symmetric-critical-point-above-theta_0-with-p14) closes it with a coupled average. *Screens*: the moment identity at a random $E_0$ matches Monte Carlo; $\min T_{15}$ is $0.27$ on singular $x$ with $u<0.885$ and $0.79$ in the band.

#### Lemma L252, the coupled fourth moment, and at $n=12$ no symmetric critical point above $\theta_0$ with $p=14$

*Derived 2026-09-26 in [the per-vector note](../reviews/2026-09-25-I73c-per-vector-rows.md). **Gate 2**: every item CONFIRMED, the identity and the reduction by [r1200](../reviews/REVIEW_r1200.md), [r1201](../reviews/REVIEW_r1201.md); the structure bound and the closure by [r1202](../reviews/REVIEW_r1202.md), [r1203](../reviews/REVIEW_r1203.md), each rebuilding the enumeration independently. Typed in [CoupledMoment.lean](../lean/HamiltonsCubic/Results/CoupledMoment.lean).*

**Statement.** (i) At every symmetric three-value critical point ([L244](#lemma-l244-the-per-vector-ceiling-on-a-symmetric-three-value-critical-point)'s setting), over the unit sphere of $E_0$,
$$(p+2)\,\mathbb E\mathop{\mathrm{tr}}x^4+2\,\mathbb ER_1=2n-\tfrac3p\lVert\Omega_0\rVert^2,\qquad\text{so}\qquad\mathbb E\big[(p+2)\mathop{\mathrm{tr}}x^4+\mu_m(x)\big]\le2n.$$
(ii) For every unit angle vector $s\in\mathbb R^6$, $T_{16}(s)\ge\min\big(\tfrac12,\ 3(1-\max_is_i^2)\big)$, $T_{16}$ the sum of the 16 smallest of the thirty values $(s_i\mp s_j)^2$. (iii) At $n=12$ every symmetric critical point with $p=14$ has $\theta<\theta_0(12)$; with L244, L250 and [L251](#lemma-l251-at-n12-the-symmetric-family-has-no-critical-point-above-theta_0-with-p15), a symmetric three-value critical point above $\theta_0(12)$ has $p\le13$.

**Proof.** (i) Average the per-vector identity $\mathop{\mathrm{tr}}(-x^2A_0)=2+2R_0-\langle x\wedge x,\Omega_0\rangle$ ([r1196](../reviews/REVIEW_r1196.md)): $\mathbb E(-x^2)=A_0/p$ and $\mathbb E\,x\wedge x=\Omega_0/p$ give $\mathop{\mathrm{tr}}A_0^2=2p+2p\,\mathbb ER_0-\lVert\Omega_0\rVert^2$. The Casimir $R_0+R_1+R_2=n-2$ and row (B) $R_2-R_0=1$ give $R_0=\tfrac12(n-3-R_1)$, and L251's $p(p+2)\,\mathbb E\mathop{\mathrm{tr}}x^4=2\mathop{\mathrm{tr}}A_0^2+2p-\lVert\Omega_0\rVert^2$ gives the identity. Ky Fan on $E_1$ gives $2R_1\ge\mu_m$. (ii) Order $s_1\ge\dots\ge s_6\ge0$ (sign changes and permutations preserve the thirty values). Every gap is at most its sum, so the 16 smallest can be chosen as doubled pairs $H_2$, free pairs $F$ with $\lvert F\rvert=\lvert H_2\rvert-1$ and single gaps on the rest of $K_6$, costing $s^{\mathsf T}Ms$ with $M=L(K_6\setminus F)+\lvert L\rvert(H_2)$. A structure missing vertex $v$ lies in the $D_5$ on the others and gives $\ge3(1-s_v^2)$ by the Gram row sum. **Certificate** ([cert_I73c_T16_structures.py](../scripts/misc/cert_I73c_T16_structures.py), from [r1203](../reviews/REVIEW_r1203.md), reproduced): $2M-I$ is positive definite at all 1,700,820 structures touching all six vertices, by integer Bareiss elimination, so $\lambda_{\min}(M)>\tfrac12>\tau$ exactly; the floating enumeration ([enum_I73c_T16_structures.py](../scripts/misc/enum_I73c_T16_structures.py)) puts the least value at $4-2\sqrt3$, one doubled pair (`one_pair_floor`), and at least $0.652$ otherwise. (iii) If $\theta\ge\theta_0(12)$, $\kappa=\theta\sqrt{28}\ge\sqrt{385}/3$ and L249 gives $T_{16}\le\tau=7-\sqrt{385}/3\approx0.4595<\tfrac12$ at every unit $x\in E_0$, so $T_{16}\ge3T$, $T\le\tau/3$ and $16\sum s^4+T_{16}\ge16\big((1-T)^2+T^2/5\big)+3T\ge12.008>12$ pointwise, against (i)'s $\mathbb E[16\sum s^4+T_{16}]\le12$. $\square$

**What it does.** (iii) closes $p=14$; (i) holds at every $n$ with no cap on $A_0$ and at $p=15$ is at least as strong as L251's average. **Owed**: a hand proof of $\lambda_{\min}(M)>\tfrac12$ for two or more doubled pairs, in place of the certificate. *At $p=13$ ($m=40$, $\mu_{40}=2T_{17}$, $\tau_{13}=7-\sqrt{220\cdot26/144}\approx0.697$) the coupled average's pointwise floor is near $22.2<24$ (screen), so (i) alone stops there; at general $n$ singular intermediate types $u(k)\oplus\mathfrak{so}(n-2k)$ have small $\mathop{\mathrm{tr}}x^4$ and defeat it.*

#### Lemma L253, $\kappa\le p-2$ on the symmetric family at every $n$, and at $n=12$ the window $8\le p\le13$

*Derived 2026-09-26 in [the per-vector note](../reviews/2026-09-25-I73c-per-vector-rows.md). **Gate 2**: [r1204](../reviews/REVIEW_r1204.md), [r1205](../reviews/REVIEW_r1205.md), independently, every item CONFIRMED, each rebuilding the enumerations. Scalar steps typed in [CoupledMoment.lean](../lean/HamiltonsCubic/Results/CoupledMoment.lean) (`small_p_scalar`, `p7_scalar`).*

**Statement.** On the symmetric three-value family at every $n$, $\kappa=\theta\sqrt{2p}\le p-2$. At $n=12$ a symmetric three-value critical point above $\theta_0(12)$ has $8\le p\le13$.

**Proof.** [L249](#lemma-l249-the-per-vector-ceiling-through-the-smallest-eigenvalues-of-mathrmad_x2) at a single unit $x\in E_0$ gives $\kappa\le\tfrac12\kappa_{2p}(x)-3$, and $\tfrac12\kappa_{2p}(x)$ is the sum of the $p$ largest root values $\langle\alpha,s\rangle^2$, since every nonzero eigenvalue of $-\mathrm{ad}_x^2$ is doubled. The Gram row sum (Gershgorin on the Gram matrix of the roots, entries $2$ or $1$ on the diagonal and at most $1$ off it) bounds any $p$ of them by $p+1$, so $\kappa\le p-2$. At $n=12$, $\theta_0^2=\tfrac{55}{36}$ and $(p-2)^2<\tfrac{55}{36}\cdot2p$ for $p\le6$. At $p=7$, any seven values sum to less than $7.6$ (**Certificate**: $38I-5G_S\succ0$ at every seven-subset of $D_6$'s roots, [cert_I73c_top7_roots.py](../scripts/misc/cert_I73c_top7_roots.py)), so $\kappa<4.6<\theta_0\sqrt{14}\approx4.6248$. With L244, L250, L251 and [L252](#lemma-l252-the-coupled-fourth-moment-and-at-n12-no-symmetric-critical-point-above-theta_0-with-p14), $8\le p\le13$. $\square$

**What it does.** It closes the bottom of the $n=12$ window. *The seven-sum maximum is exactly $4+2\sqrt3$, at a star with two doubled pairs and three single gaps; at $p=8$ the eight-sum maximum $(9+\sqrt{57})/2$ gives $5.275>4.944$, so a single element stops there.* **On the open window $8\le p\le13$ L249's region holds only near-decomposable elements** ($s_1^2\gtrsim0.8$, screen), so what remains is rigidity of a $p$-dimensional space of near-decomposable 2-forms against the cap $A_0\preceq\tfrac{11}2I$; the averaged form of that rigidity is exactly $\mathbb E\mathop{\mathrm{tr}}x^4\le24/(p+2)$, which does not close it ($\sum_{a,b}\lVert\psi_a\wedge\psi_b\rVert^2=p^2+p-\mathop{\mathrm{tr}}A_0^2$).


#### Lemma L255, the symmetric three-value family reduces to an internal bracket-mass cap, and $\theta\le4$

*Gate 2 by [r1210](../reviews/REVIEW_r1210.md) and [r1211](../reviews/REVIEW_r1211.md), independently: every claim CONFIRMED, one wording, one scope and one citation repair applied. Derived at effort `xhigh` in [the D6 working note](../reviews/2026-09-26-I73-class-ceiling.md), batch 16. The scalar core is typed in [I73InternalMass.lean](../lean/HamiltonsCubic/Results/I73InternalMass.lean) (`mass_ge_of_rows`, `sum_cube_le`, `theta_le_four`); the operator identity and the matrix identity enter as hypotheses.*

**Statement.** *Let $W$ be a unit critical point with spectrum $(-a,0,a)$, $a=1/\sqrt{2p}$, $V=E_0\oplus E_2$, $d=2p$, and $\mathcal M(V)=\sum_{u,v,w\in V}\langle[u,v],w\rangle^2$ over an orthonormal basis (record normalisation). Then*
$$\theta\ \le\ \frac{\mathcal M(V)}{2\,d^{3/2}},\qquad\mathcal M(V)=8\mathop{\mathrm{tr}}\widetilde Z^3\ \le\ 8\,d^{3/2},\qquad\text{so}\qquad\theta\le4,$$
*where $\widetilde Z=\sum_iU_i\otimes U_i$ over a Frobenius-orthonormal basis of $V$, with $\mathop{\mathrm{tr}}\widetilde Z=0$ and $\mathop{\mathrm{tr}}\widetilde Z^2=d$.*

**Proof.** With $\Pi=P_0+P_2$ and $K=1+\theta/a$, criticality $W^2+W^\#=\theta W$ at $W=a(P_2-P_0)$ is $\Pi\#\Pi=2(P_0\#P_0+P_2\#P_2)+KP_0+(2-K)P_2$. Evaluated at unit $x\in E_0$ and $x\in E_2$ through [L244](#lemma-l244-the-per-vector-ceiling-on-a-symmetric-three-value-critical-point)'s formula $\langle(P_a\#P_b)x,x\rangle=\tfrac12\sum\langle[\psi_\gamma,\psi_\delta],x\rangle^2$, it gives $\lVert P_V\mathrm{ad}_xP_V\rVert^2\ge2K$ on $E_0$ and $\ge2K-4$ on $E_2$; summed over a basis of $V$, $\mathcal M(V)\ge4p(K-1)=4p\theta\sqrt{2p}=2\theta d^{3/2}$. In class totals this is [R38](../routes.md)'s $\theta=(3\tau_{002}-3\tau_{022}+\tau_{222}-\tau_{000})/(4p\sqrt{2p})$ with $2\tau_{000}+6\tau_{022}\ge0$ dropped, and $\mathcal M=\tau_{000}+3\tau_{002}+3\tau_{022}+\tau_{222}$ is the mass of [L242](bounds-relaxations.md)'s realisability paragraph. For skew matrices $\langle[u,v],w\rangle=-\mathop{\mathrm{tr}}(uvw)$, a record-unit element is $\sqrt2$ times a Frobenius-unit one, and $\mathop{\mathrm{tr}}\widetilde Z^3=\sum_{ijk}\mathop{\mathrm{tr}}(U_iU_jU_k)^2$, so $\mathcal M=8\mathop{\mathrm{tr}}\widetilde Z^3$; $\widetilde Z$ is symmetric, so $\mathop{\mathrm{tr}}\widetilde Z^3\le(\mathop{\mathrm{tr}}\widetilde Z^2)^{3/2}=d^{3/2}$. $\square$

**What it gives.** *The per-vector obstruction ([post F77](fence-posts.md)) is replaced by one subspace inequality: **I73 on the symmetric family at $n$ follows from $\mathop{\mathrm{tr}}\widetilde Z_V^3<\tfrac{\theta_0(n)}4d^{3/2}$ for every subspace $V\subseteq\mathfrak{so}(n)$**, the internal-mass cap. The whole algebra gives $\sqrt2(n-2)/\sqrt{n(n-1)}<\theta_0(n)$ at every $n$, every subalgebra checked at $n=12$ lies below $\theta_0(12)$ ($\mathfrak{so}(12)$ $1.2309$, $\Lambda^2_+$ $1.1547$), and a Grassmannian ascent at $n=12$, $d\in\{3,6,10,15,16,20,21,26\}$, stays below $\theta_0$, its maximisers being subalgebras or subspaces of low codimension in one (at $d=6,20,26$ above every subalgebra of that dimension, [r1213](../reviews/REVIEW_r1213.md); a screen, [screen_I73_internal_mass_cap.py](../scripts/misc/screen_I73_internal_mass_cap.py)).* **Open**: the cap itself.


#### Lemma L256, the internal-mass cap on subalgebras: a closed $E_0\oplus E_2$ cannot reach $\theta_0$

*Gate 2 by [r1212](../reviews/REVIEW_r1212.md) and [r1213](../reviews/REVIEW_r1213.md), independently: the Casimir formula, the conclusion, the complement identity and the Lean CONFIRMED; one justifying clause of the index table repaired. Derived at effort `xhigh` in [the D6 working note](../reviews/2026-09-26-I73-class-ceiling.md), batch 17. The arithmetic is typed in [I73SubalgebraCap.lean](../lean/HamiltonsCubic/Results/I73SubalgebraCap.lean); the Casimir formula and the classical index table are quoted input.*

**Statement.** *For $n\ge9$ and every Lie subalgebra $\mathfrak h\subseteq\mathfrak{so}(n)$ of dimension $d$, $\mathcal M(\mathfrak h)/d^{3/2}\le2(n-2)/\sqrt N$, the value of $\mathfrak{so}(n)$. Hence, by [L255](#lemma-l255-the-symmetric-three-value-family-reduces-to-an-internal-bracket-mass-cap-and-thetale4), a symmetric three-value critical point whose $E_0\oplus E_2$ is closed under the bracket has $\theta\le\sqrt2(n-2)/\sqrt{n(n-1)}<\theta_0(n)$.*

**Proof.** On a simple ideal acting through a real representation $\rho$, $\langle X,Y\rangle=\tfrac12\ell_\rho(X,Y)$ and the Killing form is $2h^\vee(\,,)$, so an orthonormal element has internal mass $C=4h^\vee/\ell_\rho$; the centre carries none, and $\sum d_iC_i\le\max_i(C_i/\sqrt{d_i})(\sum d_i)^{3/2}$ (`ratio_le_of_parts`). Every nontrivial real irreducible has $\ell\ge2$ (the index-$1$ complex irreducibles are the $A_r$, $C_r$ fundamentals, which realify to $2$), so $C/\sqrt d\le2h^\vee/\sqrt d$: at most $4/\sqrt3$ for $A_r$ and $C_r$, $8/\sqrt{14}$ for $G_2$, below $1$ for $F_4,E_6,E_7,E_8$, and $2(k-2)/\sqrt{k(k-1)/2}$ for $\mathfrak{so}(k)$, increasing in $k\le n$ (`sok_ratio_mono`). From $n=9$, $2(n-2)/\sqrt N>4/\sqrt3$ (`sok_beats_su2`), and $\sqrt2(n-2)/\sqrt{n(n-1)}<\theta_0(n)$ (`whole_below_theta0`). $\square$

**What it gives.** *The dimension-free form of [r1125](../reviews/REVIEW_r1125.md)'s single $24$-dimensional case. The general cap is open: the screened maxima at $d=6,20,26$ are not subalgebras but subspaces of low codimension in one, above every subalgebra of their dimension and still below $\theta_0(12)$, so what remains is a stability statement over subspaces of subalgebras.*


#### Lemma L257, the internal-mass cap follows from a quartic bound on $\Pi\#\Pi$

*Gate 2 by [r1214](../reviews/REVIEW_r1214.md) and [r1215](../reviews/REVIEW_r1215.md), independently: the chain CONFIRMED, the quartic bound's range repaired to $n\ge9$. Derived at effort `xhigh` in [the D6 working note](../reviews/2026-09-26-I73-class-ceiling.md), batch 19; scalar core typed in [I73QuarticReduction.lean](../lean/HamiltonsCubic/Results/I73QuarticReduction.lean).*

**Statement.** *For every subspace $V\subseteq\mathfrak{so}(n)$ of dimension $d$ with projector $\Pi$, $\mathcal M(V)\le2\sqrt d\,\lVert\Pi\#\Pi\rVert_F$, with equality on simple subalgebras. Hence, with [L255](#lemma-l255-the-symmetric-three-value-family-reduces-to-an-internal-bracket-mass-cap-and-thetale4), I73 on the symmetric family at $n\ge12$ follows from the* **quartic bound** $\lVert\Pi\#\Pi\rVert_F\le(n-2)d/\sqrt N$ *for every rank-$d$ projector, $n\ge9$, which gives $\theta\le\sqrt2(n-2)/\sqrt{n(n-1)}<\theta_0(n)$.*

**Proof.** $K_V=\pi_V(2\,\Pi\#\Pi)\pi_V$ has $\mathop{\mathrm{tr}}K_V=\mathcal M(V)$ by [L244](#lemma-l244-the-per-vector-ceiling-on-a-symmetric-three-value-critical-point)'s formula; Cauchy–Schwarz on its $d$ eigenvalues gives $\mathcal M^2\le d\mathop{\mathrm{tr}}K_V^2$ (`sum_sq_le_card_mul`), and compression lowers the Frobenius norm of a positive operator. The chain $\theta\le\mathcal M/(2d^{3/2})\le\lVert\Pi\#\Pi\rVert_F/d\le(n-2)/\sqrt N$ is `theta_le_of_quartic`, and $(n-2)/\sqrt N<\theta_0(n)$ is `whole_below_theta0`. $\square$

**What it gives.** *The quartic bound carries no projection onto $V$: $\lVert\Pi\#\Pi\rVert_F^2=\tfrac14\sum_{i,j,k,l}\langle[v_i,v_j],[v_k,v_l]\rangle^2$ is the norm of the curvature tensor $\langle[x,y],[u,w]\rangle$ of $\mathfrak{so}(n)$ restricted to $V$, so leaks and stars no longer enter. It is false at $n\le8$ ($\Lambda^2_+$), holds on subalgebras at $n\ge9$ by [L256](#lemma-l256-the-internal-mass-cap-on-subalgebras-a-closed-e_0oplus-e_2-cannot-reach-theta_0)'s table, and three independent Grassmannian screens at $n=9,\dots,12$ found no violator (tightest $0.990$, $\Lambda^2_+$ at $n=9$; at $n=12$ at most $0.938$).* **Open: the quartic bound.**


#### Lemma L258, the quartic bound holds on every coordinate subspace

*Gate 2 by [r1218](../reviews/REVIEW_r1218.md) and [r1219](../reviews/REVIEW_r1219.md), independently: CONFIRMED, with the equality clause and one attribution repaired. [The D6 working note](../reviews/2026-09-26-I73-class-ceiling.md), batches 23 and 24; scalar chain typed in [I73CoordinateQuartic.lean](../lean/HamiltonsCubic/Results/I73CoordinateQuartic.lean).*

**Statement.** *For every graph $G$ on $n\ge3$ vertices with $e$ edges, $\sum_{i<k}\mathop{\mathrm{codeg}}(i,k)^2\le(n-2)^2e^2/N$, with equality among graphs with an edge exactly at $K_n$. Hence [L257](#lemma-l257-the-internal-mass-cap-follows-from-a-quartic-bound-on-pipi)'s quartic bound holds on every coordinate subspace $V=\mathop{\mathrm{span}}\{e_i\wedge e_j:ij\in E\}$, where $\Pi\#\Pi$ is the codegree diagonal.*

**Proof.** $\mathop{\mathrm{tr}}A^4=\sum d_i^2+2S$; the traceless fourth-moment bound $\mathop{\mathrm{tr}}A^4\le\tfrac{n^2-3n+3}{n(n-1)}(\mathop{\mathrm{tr}}A^2)^2$ (the maximum of sample kurtosis, one value against $n-1$ equal ones) and $\sum d_i^2\ge(\mathop{\mathrm{tr}}A^2)^2/n$ give $2S\le\tfrac{(n-2)^2}{n(n-1)}(2e)^2$ (`coordinate_quartic`). $\square$

**What it gives.** *The quartic bound on the whole coordinate family, at every $n\ge3$; its failure at $n\le8$ is $\Lambda^2_+$, which is not coordinate. The general case is open; its Frobenius form is $\lVert\Pi\#\Pi\rVert_F^2=8\big(\mathop{\mathrm{tr}}\widetilde Z^4-\mathop{\mathrm{tr}}(\widetilde Z^2(\widetilde Z^2)^\Gamma)\big)$, $\widetilde Z=\sum U_i\otimes U_i$, and the missing piece is the operator playing the adjacency matrix's role.*

#### Lemma L275, J1 on the face P = Q: the edge family

*Gate 2 by [r1664](../reviews/REVIEW_r1664.md) and [r1665](../reviews/REVIEW_r1665.md), independently: CONFIRMED WITH GAPS, no step refuted; repaired: the equality case, the justifications of steps (2) to (5) and of the range, and the caveat on L273 (3). Derived at the research level in [the J1 working note](../reviews/2026-09-27-I117-J1.md), batch 5; every step was screened before it was proved (minimum defect exactly $0$, at the edge). Proved in Lean as `L275_bound` in [L275Proof.lean](../lean/HamiltonsCubic/Results/L275Proof.lean), step by step (rows and pairing from [J1.lean](../lean/HamiltonsCubic/Results/J1.lean) at $V=0$, `vb` for (4), `H_pi` for (5), with `L275_pair` and `L275_close` from [L275.lean](../lean/HamiltonsCubic/Results/L275.lean)).*

**Statement.** *Let $U$ be a real symmetric $m\times m$ matrix with zero diagonal, $m\ge4$, with $x_{ij}=U_{ij}^2$, $D_i=\sum_jx_{ij}$ and $S=\sum_iD_i$. Then*
$$\mathop{\mathrm{tr}}U^3\sqrt S\le\lambda_m\sum_{i\ne j}\big(D_iD_j-x_{ij}^2\big),\qquad\lambda_m=\frac{\sqrt{2m}\,(m-2)}{(m-1)^{3/2}},$$
*Both sides vanish at a single edge, and the bound is strict at $J-I$. At $V=0$ in [L273](#lemma-l273-h-on-the-torus-slice-of-so2m-follows-for-m-at-least-5-from-one-cubic-quartic-matrix-inequality)'s coordinates ($P=Q=U$, $d_i=D_i$, $S=1$, $g^2=\sum_iD_i^2+\sum_{i\ne j}x_{ij}^2$) it is J1.*

**Proof.** *Put $\kappa=\lambda_m^2$, the walk $p_{ij}=x_{ij}/D_i$, $\pi_i=D_i/S$, $\rho_i=\sum_jp_{ij}^2$, $s=\sum_i\pi_i^2$, and $e_{ij}=D_i-x_{ij}$. (1) As in [L274](#lemma-l274-j1-at-q--0-a-degree-refined-traceless-cubic-bound) (1), $\mathop{\mathrm{tr}}U^3\le\sum_{i\ne j}\sqrt{x_{ij}e_{ij}e_{ji}}$. (2) Cauchy–Schwarz with $R_{ij}=D_iD_j-x_{ij}^2$, which satisfies $R_{ij}-e_{ij}e_{ji}=x_{ij}(e_{ij}+e_{ji})\ge0$: $\mathop{\mathrm{tr}}U^3\le\sqrt{\sum x_{ij}e_{ij}e_{ji}/R_{ij}}\sqrt{\sum R_{ij}}$, pairs with $R_{ij}=0$ contributing nothing. (3) $x\,ee'/R=x\,f(p_{ij},p_{ji})$ with $f(p,q)=(1-p)(1-q)/(1-pq)$; in $a=\tfrac{1-p}{1+p}$, $b=\tfrac{1-q}{1+q}$, $f=2ab/(a+b)\le\tfrac12(a+b)\le\tfrac12(2-p-q)$ when $a+b>0$, and $f=0$ when $a=b=0$; since $p,q\le1$ every pair has $x(1-\tfrac{p+q}2)\ge0$, so extending to all ordered pairs and symmetrising gives $\sum x\,ee'/R\le S\sum_i\pi_i(1-\rho_i)$. (4) The claim $\sum_i\pi_i(1-\rho_i)\le\kappa(1-s-\sum_i\pi_i^2\rho_i)$ is $\sum_i\pi_i(1-\kappa\pi_i)\rho_i\ge1-\kappa+\kappa s$. Both sides are invariant under scaling $U$, so take $S=1$; then its left side is $\sum_j\sum_ix_{ij}^2(1/\pi_i-\kappa)$, where $1/\pi_i-\kappa>0$ because $\pi_i\le\tfrac12$ (as in L274 (5)) and $\kappa<2$. For each $j$ with $\pi_j>0$, Cauchy–Schwarz over the $i$ with $x_{ij}>0$ gives at least $\pi_j^2/\sum_{i:x_{ij}>0}\beta_i\ge\pi_j^2/(B-\beta_j)$, $\beta_i=\pi_i/(1-\kappa\pi_i)\ge0$, $B=\sum_i\beta_i$, and $B-\beta_j>0$; the $j$ with $\pi_j=0$ contribute $0$ on both sides. (5) $\sum_j\pi_j^2/(B-\beta_j)\ge1-\kappa+\kappa s$: trivial when $s\le1-1/\kappa$. Otherwise the chord of the convex $t\mapsto1/(1-\kappa t)$ on $[0,\tfrac12]$ gives $\beta_i\le\pi_i(1+\gamma\pi_i)$, $\gamma=2\kappa/(2-\kappa)$, so $B-\beta_j\le1-\pi_j+\gamma(s-\pi_j^2)$, and Cauchy–Schwarz in Titu's form, $\sum_j\pi_j^4/(\pi_j^2c_j)\ge s^2/\sum_j\pi_j^2c_j$ with $c_j=1-\pi_j+\gamma(s-\pi_j^2)$, together with $\sum_i\pi_i^3\ge s^2$ and $\sum_i\pi_i^4\ge(\sum_i\pi_i^3)^2/s\ge s^3$ (both Cauchy–Schwarz, entering the denominator with a minus sign), gives $\sum_j\pi_j^2/(B-\beta_j)\ge s/\big((1-s)(1+\gamma s)\big)$. Multiplying out by $(1-s)(2-\kappa+2\kappa s)>0$, the inequality $s/((1-s)(1+\gamma s))\ge\kappa s+1-\kappa$ is equivalent to $(1-2s)\big[(\kappa-1)-(\kappa(1-s)-1)^2\big]\ge0$; here $1-2s\ge0$ ($s\le\max_i\pi_i\le\tfrac12$) and $\kappa(1-s)\in[\tfrac\kappa2,1]$, so the bracket is at least $\kappa-1-(1-\tfrac\kappa2)^2=-\tfrac14(\kappa^2-8\kappa+8)>0$ because $\kappa>4-2\sqrt2$. *Range:* $\kappa=\lambda_m^2=2m(m-2)^2/(m-1)^3$ is increasing in $m$, $\kappa<2$ since $(m-1)^3-m(m-2)^2=m^2-m-1>0$, and $\kappa_4=32/27>4-2\sqrt2\approx1.1716$, a thin margin; $\kappa_3=3/4$ fails. (6) Steps (3) to (5) give $\sum x\,ee'/R\le\kappa\sum R_{ij}/S$, and (2) closes.* $\square$

**What it gives.** *J1 at every torus-invariant $X$ with $V=0$ (all weights $\mu_{\varepsilon_i+\varepsilon_j}=0$), $m\ge4$, and so by L273 (3), whose proof uses J1 only at the point itself, statement H there for $m\ge5$ (the Lean `L273_J1_implies_H` takes J1 on the whole sphere, so this rests on the prose); the $\Lambda^2_+$ edge lies there. With L274 this settles J1 on both faces $Q=0$ and $P=Q$; J1 at mixed $(P,Q)$ is open.*

#### Lemma L274, J1 at Q = 0: a degree-refined traceless cubic bound

*Gate 2 by [r1662](../reviews/REVIEW_r1662.md) and [r1663](../reviews/REVIEW_r1663.md), independently: CONFIRMED WITH GAPS, no step refuted; repaired: $S>0$ in the displayed form, the containment for a general traceless matrix, the single large coordinate in (4), $D_j\le S/2$ in (5), and the edge sentence below. Derived at the research level in [the J1 working note](../reviews/2026-09-27-I117-J1.md), batch 2; every step was screened before it was proved (maximum defect exactly $0$ at $m=4$ to $10$). Proved in Lean as `L274_bound` in [L274.lean](../lean/HamiltonsCubic/Results/L274.lean), multiplied through by $\sqrt S$, one declaration per step: `rows` (1), `pairing` (2), `walk` (3), (5) and (6), `L274_simplex` (4); the typed statement checked against this block by [r1667](../reviews/REVIEW_r1667.md), CONFIRMED.*

**Statement.** *Let $W\ne0$ be a real symmetric $m\times m$ matrix with zero diagonal, $m\ge3$, with $D_i=\sum_jW_{ij}^2$ and $S=\sum_iD_i=\lVert W\rVert^2>0$. Then*
$$\mathop{\mathrm{tr}}W^3\le K_m\,\frac{S^2-\sum_iD_i^2}{\sqrt S},\qquad K_m=\frac{\sqrt m\,(m-2)}{(m-1)^{3/2}},$$
*with equality at $W=J-I$; multiplied by $\sqrt S$ it holds at $W=0$ too. Since $\sum_iD_i^2\ge S^2/m$ this contains the traceless cubic bound $\mathop{\mathrm{tr}}W^3\le\tfrac{m-2}{\sqrt{m(m-1)}}S^{3/2}$, for every real symmetric traceless matrix, which is orthogonally similar to one with zero diagonal (the zero vector is majorised by any traceless spectrum). At $Q=0$ in [L273](#lemma-l273-h-on-the-torus-slice-of-so2m-follows-for-m-at-least-5-from-one-cubic-quartic-matrix-inequality)'s coordinates ($P=W$, $\lVert W\rVert^2=2$, $d_i=D_i/2$, $\sqrt2K_m=\lambda_m$) it is J1.*

**Proof.** *Put $x_{ij}=W_{ij}^2$, $n=m-1$, and at $D_i>0$ the reversible walk $p_{ij}=x_{ij}/D_i$ with stationary law $\pi_i=D_i/S$ and edge law $x_{ij}/S$. (1) $(W^2)_{ij}=\sum_{k\ne i,j}W_{ik}W_{kj}$, so by Cauchy–Schwarz $\mathop{\mathrm{tr}}W^3\le\sum_{i\ne j}\sqrt{x_{ij}(D_i-x_{ij})(D_j-x_{ij})}$. (2) Each term is $\sqrt{x_{ij}(1-p_{ij})(1-p_{ji})}\sqrt{D_iD_j}$, and Cauchy–Schwarz over ordered pairs with $\sum_{i\ne j}D_iD_j=S^2-\sum_iD_i^2$ gives $\mathop{\mathrm{tr}}W^3\le\sqrt{S\,\mathbb E[(1-p_{ij})(1-p_{ji})]}\sqrt{S^2-\sum_iD_i^2}$. (3) $(1-p_{ij})(1-p_{ji})\le\tfrac12(1-p_{ij})^2+\tfrac12(1-p_{ji})^2$ and the edge law is symmetric, so the expectation is at most $\sum_i\pi_ih(p_{i\cdot})$, $h(p)=\sum_kp_k(1-p_k)^2$. (4) For a probability vector $p$ on $n\ge2$ points, $h(p)\le\big(\tfrac{n-1}n\big)^2-\tfrac{n-1}n\big(\sum_kp_k^2-\tfrac1n\big)$: this is $\sum_kp_k(1-p_k)(np_k-1)\ge0$, whose left side is $n\sum_k(p_k-\tfrac1n)^2(1-\tfrac1n-p_k)$ because $\sum_k(p_k-\tfrac1n)=0$; every term is non-negative unless some $p_1=a>1-\tfrac1n$, and at most one coordinate can be (two would sum past $2-\tfrac2n\ge1$); then the others satisfy $1-\tfrac1n-p_k\ge a-\tfrac1n$, so Cauchy–Schwarz on $\sum_{k\ge2}(p_k-\tfrac1n)=\tfrac1n-a$ leaves at least $(a-\tfrac1n)^2\tfrac{n-2}{n-1}(1-a)\ge0$. (5) At $S=1$, $\sum_i\pi_i\sum_jp_{ij}^2=\sum_j\sum_ix_{ij}^2/D_i\ge\sum_jD_j^2/(1-D_j)$ by Cauchy–Schwarz over the $i$ with $x_{ij}>0$, whose $D_i$ sum to at most $1-D_j$; here $D_j\le\tfrac12<1$, since $S\ge D_j+\sum_{i\ne j}x_{ij}=2D_j$, and $\psi(t)=t^2/(1-t)-\tfrac{n^2-1}{n^2}t^2$ is convex on $[0,1)$, so Jensen over the $n+1$ vertices gives at least $(n+1)\psi(\tfrac1{n+1})+\tfrac{n^2-1}{n^2}\sum\pi_i^2=\big(1+(n^2-1)\sum_i\pi_i^2\big)/n^2$. (6) Steps 3 to 5 give $\mathbb E[\cdot]\le K_m^2(1-\sum_i\pi_i^2)$ with $K_m^2=(n+1)(n-1)^2/n^3$, and step 2 closes.* $\square$

**What it gives.** *J1 on the $Q=0$ part of the torus slice, so by L273 (3), whose proof uses J1 only at the point itself, statement H holds at every torus-invariant $X\in\mathop{\mathrm{Sym}}^2\mathfrak{so}(2m)$ with $\mu_{\varepsilon_i+\varepsilon_j}=\mu_{\varepsilon_i-\varepsilon_j}$ for all $i<j$, $m\ge5$; the uniform point lies there. J1 at $Q\ne0$ is open. The same row bound on the $2m\times2m$ cocktail-party matrix $\begin{pmatrix}U&V\\V&U\end{pmatrix}$, and the separate bound $T\le\tfrac12(\Gamma(P)+\Gamma(Q))$ with $\Gamma$ step (1)'s right side, both screen exact on it at $m=4$ to $8$; pairing with $\sqrt{d_id_j}$ in step (2) fails there by about $0.05$, so the $\sum_{i\ne j}P_{ij}^2Q_{ij}^2$ part of $1-g^2$ has to enter the pairing ([working note](../reviews/2026-09-27-I117-J1.md), batch 3). At a single-edge $W$, the $Q=0$ shadow of the $\Lambda^2_+$ edge, every step but Jensen in (5) is an equality. The Lean `L273_J1_implies_H` takes J1 on the whole sphere, so the corollary for the $Q=0$ part rests on the prose of L273 (3).*

#### Lemma L287, an absolute trace floor at every positive-semidefinite idempotent, so H asks nothing of an embedding of large index, the four heaviest exceptional algebras among them

*Gate 2 by [r1910](../reviews/REVIEW_r1910.md) and [r1911](../reviews/REVIEW_r1911.md), independently: CONFIRMED on both parts, their correction (every $\mathfrak g_2$ embedding with $j\ge2$ passes) and [r1911](../reviews/REVIEW_r1911.md)'s direct route through $\mathop{\mathrm{tr}}C\le c\lVert C\rVert^2$ folded. Derived at the research level in [the I117 working note](../reviews/2026-09-28l-I117-g2-torus.md). Proved in Lean as `index_H` in [ExceptionalH.lean](../lean/HamiltonsCubic/Results/ExceptionalH.lean), through `trace_floor`, `trace_ge`, `norm_floor`, `index_form` and `requirement_lt`, with the criterion at each algebra as `exceptional_pass`. The eigenbasis identity, $\lvert\theta\rvert^2=2c/h^\vee$, the pairing $\mathop{\mathrm{tr}}C\le c\lVert C\rVert^2$, $c=h^\vee/j$ and the minimal indices enter as hypotheses.*

**Statement.** *(1) Every nonzero positive-semidefinite $\#$-idempotent $C$ on a compact simple $\mathfrak h$, in the metric where $I^\#I=cI$, has $\mathop{\mathrm{tr}}C\ge h^\vee/c$ and $\lVert C\rVert^2\ge h^\vee/c^2$. (2) In an embedding $\mathfrak h\subset\mathfrak{so}(n)$ of Dynkin index $j$ (the adjoint's being $h^\vee$), $c=h^\vee/j$ and $\lVert C\rVert^2\ge j^2/h^\vee$. So where $j^2\ge\tfrac34h^\vee$ and $n\ge9$, every positive-definite $\#$-idempotent on $\mathfrak h$ has $\lVert C\rVert^2\ge N/(n-2)^2$: H asks nothing of $\mathfrak h$, on or off the torus. This covers $\mathfrak f_4,\mathfrak e_6,\mathfrak e_7,\mathfrak e_8$ in every embedding, $\mathfrak g_2$ in every embedding other than $7$ plus trivial summands, and every $\mathfrak h$ in its adjoint embedding.*

**Proof.** *(1) On an orthonormal eigenbasis, $C=\sum_i\lambda_iu_iu_i^{\mathsf T}$ with $\lambda_i\ge0$, and $(vv^{\mathsf T})^\#(ww^{\mathsf T})=\tfrac12[v,w][v,w]^{\mathsf T}$ makes the diagonal of $C^\#C=C$ at $u_m$ read $\lambda_m=\tfrac12\sum_{i,j}c_{ijm}\lambda_i\lambda_j$, $c_{ijm}=\langle[u_i,u_j],u_m\rangle^2$, with $\sum_ic_{ijm}=\lVert[u_j,u_m]\rVert^2$ by invariance. At the largest $\lambda_m>0$ this gives $2\le\lvert\theta\rvert^2\mathop{\mathrm{tr}}C$, since $\max_{\lvert x\rvert=1}\lVert\mathrm{ad}_x\rVert^2=\lvert\theta\rvert^2$ on a Cartan. Comparing $\mathop{\mathrm{tr}}(\mathrm{ad}_x^{\mathsf T}\mathrm{ad}_x)=2c\lvert x\rvert^2$ ([L260](#lemma-l260-statement-h-reduces-to-the-least-norm-of-a-positive-definite--idempotent-on-a-semisimple-subalgebra)'s $\sum_{i,j}c_{ijk}=2c$) with $\sum_\alpha\alpha(x)^2=h^\vee\lvert\theta\rvert^2\lvert x\rvert^2$ gives $\lvert\theta\rvert^2=2c/h^\vee$. Pairing $C^\#C=C$ with $I$, $\mathop{\mathrm{tr}}C=\langle I^\#C,C\rangle\le c\lVert C\rVert^2$, as [L265](#lemma-l265-where-ic-is-stable-i-acts-on-each-component-of-sym2-h-by-c-minus-a-quarter-of-its-casimir)'s scalars $c-\mathrm{Cas}_V/4$ are at most $c$. (2) Through a real representation of index $j$, $c=h^\vee/j$, so $h^\vee/c^2=j^2/h^\vee\ge\tfrac34>N/(n-2)^2$ at $n\ge9$. The index is additive over summands, and the smallest real ones are $\mathfrak f_4$'s $26$ ($j=3$, $h^\vee=9$), $\mathfrak e_6$'s $27\oplus\overline{27}$ ($6$, $12$), $\mathfrak e_7$'s quaternionic $56$ taken twice ($12$, $18$), $\mathfrak e_8$'s $248$ ($30$, $30$); $\mathfrak g_2$'s $7$ is its only representation of index $1$ ($h^\vee=4$), and the adjoint has $j=h^\vee$.* $\square$

**What it gives.** *H asks nothing of $\mathfrak f_4$, $\mathfrak e_6$, $\mathfrak e_7$, $\mathfrak e_8$, so [I117](../plan.md)'s owed list is the classical algebras and $\mathfrak g_2\subset\mathfrak{so}(7)$, all at index one, where $j^2/h^\vee=1/h^\vee$ falls short. The floor is a fraction $h^\vee/d$ of $I/c$'s trace, so it leaves U's absolute lower bound $\mathop{\mathrm{tr}}C\ge d/c$ open. With [L266](#lemma-l266-two-constraints-on-the-maximiser-the-cubic-vanishes-on-orbit-tangents-and-a-proper-support-caps-the-trace-by-each-complementary-casimir) (2) it caps the complementary Casimirs of a proper simple support at $\mathrm{Cas}_{\mathfrak h}(V)\le dc/h^\vee$.*

#### Lemma L286, H asks nothing more of g2's torus family: its positive idempotents have trace at least 2, above the threshold 12/7 of the pairing with I

*Gate 2 by [r1896](../reviews/REVIEW_r1896.md) and [r1897](../reviews/REVIEW_r1897.md) on the trace threshold, and by [r1899](../reviews/REVIEW_r1899.md) and [r1900](../reviews/REVIEW_r1900.md) on the trace bound, independently: CONFIRMED, corrections folded. Derived at the research level in [the I117 working note](../reviews/2026-09-28l-I117-g2-torus.md). Proved in Lean as `g2_torus_trace` and `g2_torus_H` in [G2TorusTrace.lean](../lean/HamiltonsCubic/Results/G2TorusTrace.lean), through `row_bound`, `weight_lt`, `trace_of_row`, and `norm_ge_of_pairing`, `g2_threshold` in [TraceThreshold.lean](../lean/HamiltonsCubic/Results/TraceThreshold.lean). The torus rows (product table, [r1884](../reviews/REVIEW_r1884.md), [r1885](../reviews/REVIEW_r1885.md)) and the pairing identity ([L265](#lemma-l265-where-ic-is-stable-i-acts-on-each-component-of-sym2-h-by-c-minus-a-quarter-of-its-casimir)'s scalars) enter as hypotheses.*

**Statement.** *(1) On a compact simple $\mathfrak h$ with $I^\#I=cI$, $d=\dim\mathfrak h$ and $\gamma^*>0$ the largest scalar $c-\mathrm{Cas}_V/4$ over nontrivial $V\subseteq\mathop{\mathrm{Sym}}^2\mathfrak h$, every $\#$-idempotent with $\tau=\mathop{\mathrm{tr}}C$ has $\gamma^*\lVert C\rVert^2\ge\tau-(c-\gamma^*)\tau^2/d$ and $\lVert C\rVert^2\ge\tau^2/d$. On $\mathfrak g_2$ ($c=4$, $d=14$, $\gamma^*=\tfrac53$) every idempotent with $\mathop{\mathrm{tr}}C\ge\tfrac{12}7$ has $\lVert C\rVert^2\ge\tfrac{36}{49}$, H's requirement at every $n\ge9$. (2) Every positive-definite torus-invariant idempotent of $\mathfrak g_2$ has $\mathop{\mathrm{tr}}C\ge2$, hence $\lVert C\rVert^2\ge\tfrac45$.*

**Proof.** *(1) Pair $C^\#C=C$ with $I$: $\tau=c\tau^2/d+\sum_V\gamma_V\lVert C_V\rVert^2\le c\tau^2/d+\gamma^*(\lVert C\rVert^2-\tau^2/d)$; the threshold is the smaller root against $\tfrac{36}{49}$, and $\tau^2/14$ covers from the larger. (2) With long weights $x_i$, short $y_i$, $L_i\perp S_i$, the short rows are $y_i(1-\sigma_i)=x_ky_j+x_jy_k+\tfrac43y_jy_k$, $\sigma_i=x_j^2+x_k^2+\tfrac49y_i^2+\tfrac19(y_j^2+y_k^2)$. At the largest $y_i$ the right side is at most $y_i(x_j+x_k+\tfrac23(y_j+y_k))$, so $G_i=x_j+x_k+\tfrac23(y_j+y_k)+\sigma_i\ge1$, and $\mathop{\mathrm{tr}}C=\sum_\alpha(2w_\alpha+\lvert\alpha\rvert^2w_\alpha^2)\ge2G_i$ term by term.* $\square$

**What it gives.** *H asks nothing more of $\mathfrak g_2$'s torus family, whatever a solution's stabiliser, so the trivial-stabiliser type needs no enumeration for H; the sharp U$_{\mathrm s}$ ($\lVert C\rVert^2\ge\tfrac78$) stays open there. The same row argument on $\mathfrak{sp}(k)$'s $p$- and $q$-rows gives $\mathop{\mathrm{tr}}C\ge2$ (`sp_trace_ge_two` in [SpTorusTrace.lean](../lean/HamiltonsCubic/Results/SpTorusTrace.lean)), above $\mathfrak{sp}(k)$'s threshold at $k=2,3$ (the comparison prose, both readers): H asks nothing more of $\mathfrak{sp}(2)$'s and $\mathfrak{sp}(3)$'s torus families.*

#### Lemma L285, on sp(k)'s torus family the equal-weight sector holds only I/c

*Gate 2 by [r1888](../reviews/REVIEW_r1888.md) and [r1889](../reviews/REVIEW_r1889.md), independently: CONFIRMED on the torus system at every $k$ and on every step. Lean fidelity by [r1891](../reviews/REVIEW_r1891.md) and [r1892](../reviews/REVIEW_r1892.md), one reader each, CONFIRMED, their prose corrections folded. Derived at the research level in [the I117 working note](../reviews/2026-09-28l-I117-g2-torus.md). Proved in Lean as `equal_weight_torus` in [SpTorusEqualWeight.lean](../lean/HamiltonsCubic/Results/SpTorusEqualWeight.lean), through `equal_weight_unique`, `square_completion`, `u_equal` and `eigvec_proportional`. The derivation of the displayed rows from $C^\#C=C$ is prose, from both readers.*

**Statement.** *In $\mathfrak{so}(4k)$'s metric let $C$ on $\mathfrak{sp}(k)$ be torus-invariant, with long weights $u_i$ on $2\varepsilon_i$, short weights $p_{ij}$ on $\varepsilon_i-\varepsilon_j$ and $q_{ij}$ on $\varepsilon_i+\varepsilon_j$, and Cartan part $A$ with $a_i=A_{ii}=2u_i^2+\tfrac12\sum_j(p_{ij}^2+q_{ij}^2)$, $A_{ij}=\tfrac12(q_{ij}^2-p_{ij}^2)$. Then $C^\#C=C$ reads*
$$u_i(1-2a_i)=\sum_jp_{ij}q_{ij},\quad p_{ij}\big(1-X^-_{ij}\big)=(u_i+u_j)q_{ij}+\tfrac12\sum_l(p_{il}p_{jl}+q_{il}q_{jl}),\quad q_{ij}\big(1-X^+_{ij}\big)=(u_i+u_j)p_{ij}+\tfrac12\sum_l(p_{il}q_{jl}+q_{il}p_{jl}),$$
*$X^\pm_{ij}=\tfrac12(a_i+a_j)\pm\tfrac12(q_{ij}^2-p_{ij}^2)$, and every positive solution with $p_{ij}=q_{ij}$ for all $i\ne j$ has $u_i=p_{ij}=q_{ij}=\tfrac1{k+1}$, which is $I/c$, $c=k+1$.*

**Proof.** *With $S=2P$ symmetric with zero diagonal and $U=\mathop{\mathrm{diag}}(u)$: (1) the long rows give $(u_i-a_i)(1+2u_i)=0$, so $a_i=u_i\le\tfrac12$; (2) the $p$-rows become $S^2=2S-3(US+SU)+4U-8U^2$; (3) $T=S+3U-I$ has $T^2=(I-U)^2$; (4) $T$ commutes with the diagonal $T^2$ and $T_{ij}=s_{ij}>0$, so all $u_i$ equal $u$; (5) the columns of $M=S+2uI$ are positive eigenvectors of $S$ for $2-4u$; (6) two positive eigenvectors for one eigenvalue are proportional, by the minimum ratio $t=\min_ix_i/y_i$ and $\sum_{l\ne m}s_{ml}(x_l-ty_l)=0$; (7) so $M=2uJ$, and the diagonal of $SM=(2-4u)M$ gives $u=\tfrac1{k+1}$, $s=\tfrac2{k+1}$.* $\square$

**What it gives.** *[L265](#lemma-l265-where-ic-is-stable-i-acts-on-each-component-of-sym2-h-by-c-minus-a-quarter-of-its-casimir) makes $I/c$ unstable on $\mathfrak{sp}(k)$, so U$_{\mathrm s}'$ on $\mathfrak{sp}(k)$'s torus family holds wherever every positive solution has $p=q$. That $d=p-q$ vanishes is proved near $I/c$ by the working note's trace identity and is open globally.*

#### Lemma L283, su(k)'s torus family sits in so(2k)'s torus slice, so it obeys so(2M)'s bound for k at most M, M at least 7

*Gate 2 by [r1727](../reviews/REVIEW_r1727.md) and [r1728](../reviews/REVIEW_r1728.md), independently: CONFIRMED on the restriction, the torus invariance, the use of L282 and the coordinates; three wording gaps repaired in the note. Derived at the research level in [the R48 working note](../reviews/2026-09-27-R48-torus-families.md), batch 15. Proved in Lean as `H_su_slice`, `torusOp_kills_anti`, `torusOp_commutes_J`, `torusOp_kills_J` and `torusOp_perp_J` in [SuTorus.lean](../lean/HamiltonsCubic/Results/SuTorus.lean), the converse as `su_rep` and the bound at every such operator as `H_su_op` in [SuRep.lean](../lean/HamiltonsCubic/Results/SuRep.lean), through `L268_rep`. The intrinsic cubic form of $\mathfrak{su}(k)$, and the zero extension from $SU(k)$'s torus-invariant operators on $\mathfrak{su}(k)$ to $\mathfrak{so}(2k)$'s torus-invariant operators (the hypothesis of `su_rep`, [r1758](../reviews/REVIEW_r1758.md)), are prose.*

**Statement.** *Let $\mathfrak{su}(k)\subset\mathfrak u(k)\subset\mathfrak{so}(2k)$ be the standard embedding, $J$ the complex structure. In [L271](#lemma-l271-the-torus-system-is-two-coupled-copies-of-l261s-exchanged-by-p-and-q)'s coordinates, for $A$ symmetric with $A\mathbf 1=0$ and $U$ symmetric with zero diagonal, the torus-invariant operator `torusOp A U 0` kills every bivector anticommuting with $J$, and its image commutes with $J$ and is orthogonal to $J$, so it is supported on $\mathfrak{su}(k)$; conversely every torus-invariant self-adjoint operator on $\mathfrak{so}(2k)$ that kills every bivector anticommuting with $J$ and kills $J$ is such a `torusOp A U 0`. For $M\ge7$ and $k\le M$ each has $F(C)\le\kappa_{2M}\lVert C\rVert^3$.*

**Proof.** *The roots $\varepsilon_i-\varepsilon_j$ are the $J$-commuting planes and $\varepsilon_i+\varepsilon_j$ the anticommuting ones, so with $V=0$ every anticommuting block is sent to zero and every image block commutes with $J$; $A\mathbf1=0$ kills the Cartan direction $J=\sum_ph_p$, and self-adjointness makes the image orthogonal to it. The bound is [L282](#lemma-l282-every-sok-torus-slice-with-k-at-most-2m-obeys-so2ms-bound-for-m-at-least-7) (1) at size $k\le M$ with $V=0$.* $\square$

**What it gives.** *These are exactly $\mathfrak{su}(k)$'s torus family, with $\mathfrak{su}(k)$'s own cubic form (batch 15, prose): an operator $C$ on $\mathfrak{su}(k)$ invariant under $SU(k)$'s torus $T'$, extended by zero to $\hat C$, is invariant under $U(k)$'s torus $T=T'Z$, a maximal torus of $SO(2k)$, because the centre $Z$ acts trivially on $\mathfrak u(k)$; it is then `torusOp A U 0` with $A\mathbf1=0$, the roots $\varepsilon_i-\varepsilon_j$ staying distinct on $T'$; and in an orthonormal basis adapted to $\mathfrak{su}(k)\oplus\mathfrak{su}(k)^\perp$ only $\mathfrak{su}(k)$'s own brackets enter $F(\hat C)$, so $F(\hat C)=F_{\mathfrak{su}(k)}(C)$. So for $n\ge14$ and $\mathfrak{su}(k)$ standardly embedded in $\mathfrak{so}(n)$ ($2k\le n$), $M=\lfloor n/2\rfloor$ gives $F_{\mathfrak{su}(k)}(C)\le\kappa_n\lVert C\rVert^3$ on its torus family, $\kappa$ increasing in the dimension, and $\lVert C\rVert^2\ge N/(n-2)^2$ at its torus-invariant idempotents: the standard $\mathfrak{su}(k)$ torus families ask nothing more of H at $n\ge14$. H with $\mathfrak{su}(k)$'s own constant fails on its slice (batch 1), consistently, since H at $n$ asks only for the ambient one. The argument needs $\mathfrak h$'s torus and its centraliser's to fill a maximal torus of an $\mathfrak{so}(n')\supseteq\mathfrak h$; $\mathfrak{sp}(k)\subset\mathfrak{so}(4k)$, $k\ge2$, and $\mathfrak g_2\subset\mathfrak{so}(7)$ fail that count and stay open.*

#### Lemma L282, every so(k) torus slice with k at most 2M obeys so(2M)'s bound for M at least 7

*Gate 2 by [r1724](../reviews/REVIEW_r1724.md) and [r1725](../reviews/REVIEW_r1725.md), independently: CONFIRMED on the embedding, both parts and the Lean statements; the $n=12$ sentence's citation and the $n=13$ scope repaired in the note. Derived at the research level in [the R48 working note](../reviews/2026-09-27-R48-torus-families.md), batch 14. Proved in Lean as `H_small_even` and `H_small_odd` in [TorusEmbed.lean](../lean/HamiltonsCubic/Results/TorusEmbed.lean); (1) at every torus-invariant operator as `H_small_even_op` in [TorusLift.lean](../lean/HamiltonsCubic/Results/TorusLift.lean), through `L268_rep`; (2) likewise as `H_small_odd_op` in [OddRep.lean](../lean/HamiltonsCubic/Results/OddRep.lean), through `L280_rep`.*

**Statement.** *Let $M\ge7$ and $\kappa_{2M}=\tfrac{2(M-1)}{\sqrt{M(2M-1)}}$. (1) For $m\le M$, every torus-invariant $C\in\mathop{\mathrm{Sym}}^2\mathfrak{so}(2m)$ has $F(C)\le\kappa_{2M}\lVert C\rVert^3$. (2) For $m+1\le M$, every torus-invariant $C\in\mathop{\mathrm{Sym}}^2\mathfrak{so}(2m+1)$ has $F(C)\le\kappa_{2M}\lVert C\rVert^3$.*

**Proof.** *Border [L271](#lemma-l271-the-torus-system-is-two-coupled-copies-of-l261s-exchanged-by-p-and-q)'s coordinates on $\mathfrak{so}(2m)$'s slice: $A$ by a zero row and column, $U$ and $V$ by one vector $z$ on a new index with a zero corner. Then $P'$ is $P$ bordered by $2z$, $Q'$ is $Q$ bordered by $0$, $d'_i=d_i+2z_i^2$, and $A'$ vanishes on the new index, so $\mathop{\mathrm{tr}}(\mathop{\mathrm{bord}}X)^3=\mathop{\mathrm{tr}}X^3+3y^{\mathsf T}Xy$ gives L271's form at the bordered triple as $3\sum_iA_{ii}(d_i+2z_i^2)-3\sum_{i\ne j}A_{ij}P_{ij}Q_{ij}+\tfrac12(\mathop{\mathrm{tr}}P^3+\mathop{\mathrm{tr}}Q^3)+6z^{\mathsf T}Pz$, and its norm as $\lVert C\rVert^2+4\lVert z\rVert^2$. (1) At $z=0$ nothing changes, so iterating up to size $M$ and applying [L277](#lemma-l277-j1-holds-for-m-at-least-7-so-h-holds-on-the-torus-slice-of-so2m) there gives the bound. (2) At $z=w/\sqrt2$ the bordered form and norm are [L280](#lemma-l280-the-cubic-form-on-the-torus-slice-of-so2m1)'s on $\mathfrak{so}(2m+1)$, and (1) at size $m+1$ finishes.* $\square$

**What it gives.** *$\kappa_n$ increases in $n$, so with $M=\lfloor n/2\rfloor$ every standardly embedded $\mathfrak{so}(k)\subsetneq\mathfrak{so}(n)$, $n\ge14$, has $F(C)\le\kappa_n\lVert C\rVert^3$ on its torus family, hence $\lVert C\rVert^2\ge N/(n-2)^2$ at its idempotents, which is all H at $n$ asks of a proper subalgebra ([L270](#lemma-l270-the-direction-i-destabilises-only-idempotents-heavier-than-ic-and-h-asks-only-that-stable-idempotents-are-not-lighter) (3) with [L256](#lemma-l256-the-internal-mass-cap-on-subalgebras-a-closed-e_0oplus-e_2-cannot-reach-theta_0)). With [L277](#lemma-l277-j1-holds-for-m-at-least-7-so-h-holds-on-the-torus-slice-of-so2m) and [L281](#lemma-l281-h-holds-on-the-torus-slice-of-so2m1-for-m-at-least-7) for $k=n$, the torus families of the standardly embedded $\mathfrak{so}(k)$ ask nothing more of H at any $n\ge14$. At $n=12$ and $n=13$ the same embedding reduces every $k\le12$ to $\mathfrak{so}(12)$'s slice, where MW' at $m=6$ rests on one machine certificate ([the J1 working note](../reviews/2026-09-27-I117-J1.md), batch 13) and no numbered result covers it, and $k=13$ at $n=13$ is the odd slice at $m=6$, below L281's range.*

#### Lemma L281, H holds on the torus slice of so(2m+1) for m at least 7

*Gate 2 by [r1714](../reviews/REVIEW_r1714.md) and [r1715](../reviews/REVIEW_r1715.md), independently: CONFIRMED (the assembly in [the R48 working note](../reviews/2026-09-27-R48-torus-families.md), batch 12). Proved in Lean as `H_torus_slice_odd` in [OddTorus.lean](../lean/HamiltonsCubic/Results/OddTorus.lean), through the closed form below in place of batch 11's parametrisation of $\Psi_\kappa$, and at every torus-invariant operator as `H_torus_slice_odd_op` in [OddRep.lean](../lean/HamiltonsCubic/Results/OddRep.lean) through `L280_rep`.*

**Statement.** *For $m\ge7$ and every torus-invariant $C\in\mathop{\mathrm{Sym}}^2\mathfrak{so}(2m+1)$, $F(C)\le\kappa\lVert C\rVert^3$ with $\kappa=\tfrac{2m-1}{\sqrt{m(2m+1)}}=\tfrac{n-2}{\sqrt{\dim\mathfrak{so}(n)}}$, $n=2m+1$: statement H on the torus slice of $\mathfrak{so}(n)$ at odd $n\ge15$.*

**Proof.** *By [L280](#lemma-l280-the-cubic-form-on-the-torus-slice-of-so2m1), $F=3\langle A,A_\mu\rangle+T$ with $T=\tfrac12(\mathop{\mathrm{tr}}P^3+\mathop{\mathrm{tr}}Q^3)+3w^{\mathsf T}Pw$ and $A_\mu$ the matrix with diagonal $d_i+w_i^2$ and off-diagonal $-P_{ij}Q_{ij}$, so $\lVert A_\mu\rVert=g$, and $\lVert C\rVert^2=a^2+b^2$ with $a=\lVert A\rVert$, $b^2=\lVert U\rVert^2+\lVert V\rVert^2+2\lVert w\rVert^2$. At $b=0$, $F=0$. Otherwise [L279](#lemma-l279-j1-on-the-torus-slice-of-so2m1-for-m-at-least-7) on $(U,V,w)/b$ gives $T\le c_0b^3-\lambda g^2/b$, Cauchy–Schwarz gives $3\langle A,A_\mu\rangle\le3ag$, and $3ag-\lambda g^2/b\le\tfrac{9a^2b}{4\lambda}$, so $F\le b\,(c_0b^2+\tfrac{9a^2}{4\lambda})$. With $x=a^2$, $y=b^2$,*
$$\kappa^2(x+y)^3-y\Big(c_0y+\frac{9x}{4\lambda}\Big)^2=\frac{(2m-1)^2(2mx-y)^2\big(8mx+(6m-1)y\big)}{32m^4(2m+1)}\ge0,$$
*so $F\le\kappa(a^2+b^2)^{3/2}$.* $\square$

**What it gives.** *Statement H on the torus family of $\mathfrak{so}(n)$ for odd $n\ge15$, the odd counterpart of [L277](#lemma-l277-j1-holds-for-m-at-least-7-so-h-holds-on-the-torus-slice-of-so2m)'s even $n\ge14$. Equality holds only at $y=2mx$, the uniform point's ratio $\lVert A\rVert^2/b^2=\tfrac1{2m}$. Odd $n$ from $9$ to $13$ ($m=4$ to $6$) stay open, since L278 and so L279 start at $m=7$.*

#### Lemma L280, the cubic form on the torus slice of so(2m+1)

*Gate 2 by [r1714](../reviews/REVIEW_r1714.md) and [r1715](../reviews/REVIEW_r1715.md), independently: CONFIRMED on every step, the coordinate diagonal of L271's operator checked directly by both. Derived at the research level in [the R48 working note](../reviews/2026-09-27-R48-torus-families.md), batch 12, where it replaces batch 5's fitted formula. Proved in Lean as `L280_F` and `L280_norm` in [OddTorus.lean](../lean/HamiltonsCubic/Results/OddTorus.lean), over `torusOpOdd`, L271's operator on $\mathfrak{so}(2m)$ extended by zero plus the short-root part; the statement's first clause, that every torus-invariant symmetric $C$ is of this form, is `L280_rep` in [OddRep.lean](../lean/HamiltonsCubic/Results/OddRep.lean), so L280 is typed in full.*

**Statement.** *On $\mathfrak{so}(2m+1)$ with $\langle X,Y\rangle=\tfrac12\mathop{\mathrm{tr}}(X^{\mathsf T}Y)$, Cartan $\mathop{\mathrm{span}}(e_{2k-1}\wedge e_{2k})$ and short root planes $R_{\varepsilon_i}=\mathop{\mathrm{span}}\{e_{2i-1}\wedge e_{2m+1},\,e_{2i}\wedge e_{2m+1}\}$, every torus-invariant symmetric $C$ is $A\oplus\bigoplus_\alpha\mu_\alpha I_{R_\alpha}$. With $U_{ij}=\mu_{\varepsilon_i-\varepsilon_j}$, $V_{ij}=\mu_{\varepsilon_i+\varepsilon_j}$, $w_i=\mu_{\varepsilon_i}$, $P=U+V$, $Q=U-V$ and $d_i=\sum_l(U_{il}^2+V_{il}^2)$,*
$$\lVert C\rVert^2=\lVert A\rVert^2+\lVert U\rVert^2+\lVert V\rVert^2+2\lVert w\rVert^2,\qquad F(C)=3\sum_iA_{ii}(d_i+w_i^2)-3\sum_{i\ne j}A_{ij}P_{ij}Q_{ij}+\tfrac12(\mathop{\mathrm{tr}}P^3+\mathop{\mathrm{tr}}Q^3)+3\,w^{\mathsf T}Pw .$$

**Proof.** *Write $C=E+S$ with $E$ the Cartan and long-root part, supported on $\mathfrak{so}(2m)$ and zero on $\mathfrak p=\mathop{\mathrm{span}}\{e_a\wedge e_{2m+1}\}$, and $S=\bigoplus_iw_iI_{R_{\varepsilon_i}}$, supported on $\mathfrak p$ and zero on $\mathfrak{so}(2m)$. The supports are orthogonal, which gives the norm with [L271](#lemma-l271-the-torus-system-is-two-coupled-copies-of-l261s-exchanged-by-p-and-q) (1). In an orthonormal basis $F(X,Y,Z)=\tfrac12\sum_{\beta,\gamma}\langle Y[Xe_\beta,Ze_\gamma],[e_\beta,e_\gamma]\rangle$, so $F(X,Y,Z)=0$ whenever $Y$ kills every bracket $[Xu,Zv]$. Since $[\mathfrak{so}(2m),\mathfrak{so}(2m)]\subseteq\mathfrak{so}(2m)$, $[\mathfrak{so}(2m),\mathfrak p]\subseteq\mathfrak p$ and $[\mathfrak p,\mathfrak p]\subseteq\mathfrak{so}(2m)$, the terms $F(E,E,S)$, $F(E,S,E)$, $F(S,E,E)$ and $F(S,S,S)$ vanish, each with the operator that kills the bracket in the middle, and symmetry turns $F(E,S,S)$ and $F(S,S,E)$ into $F(S,E,S)$. So $F(C)=F(E)+3F(S,E,S)$. $F(E)$ is L271 (1)'s value, the brackets and inner products of $\mathfrak{so}(2m)$ being its own. In $F(S,E,S)=\tfrac12\sum w_\beta w_\gamma\langle E[e_\beta,e_\gamma],[e_\beta,e_\gamma]\rangle$ over the coordinate vectors of $\mathfrak p$, $[e_a\wedge e_{2m+1},e_b\wedge e_{2m+1}]=\pm e_a\wedge e_b$, and $E$'s diagonal entry there is $A_{ii}$ on $e_{2i-1}\wedge e_{2i}$ and $\tfrac12P_{ij}$ on the coordinate vectors joining planes $i\ne j$, so $F(S,E,S)=\sum_iw_i^2A_{ii}+w^{\mathsf T}Pw$.* $\square$

**What it gives.** *The odd slice is J1's with the Cartan weight shifted to $d_i+w_i^2$ and one new cubic $3w^{\mathsf T}Pw$, from the triangles $\{\varepsilon_i,\varepsilon_j,\varepsilon_i\pm\varepsilon_j\}$ through two short roots. With [L279](#lemma-l279-j1-on-the-torus-slice-of-so2m1-for-m-at-least-7) it gives [L281](#lemma-l281-h-holds-on-the-torus-slice-of-so2m1-for-m-at-least-7).*

#### Lemma L279, J1 on the torus slice of so(2m+1) for m at least 7

*Gate 2 by [r1708](../reviews/REVIEW_r1708.md) and [r1709](../reviews/REVIEW_r1709.md), independently: CONFIRMED on every step; Lean fidelity CONFIRMED by [r1713](../reviews/REVIEW_r1713.md). Derived at the research level in [the R48 working note](../reviews/2026-09-27-R48-torus-families.md), batch 11. Proved in Lean as `J1odd_holds` in [OddJ1.lean](../lean/HamiltonsCubic/Results/OddJ1.lean), one declaration per step, closing on L278's `oddMWprime`.*

**Statement.** *Let $m\ge7$, $U,V$ symmetric real $m\times m$ matrices with zero diagonal, $w\in\mathbb R^m$, $P=U+V$, $Q=U-V$, $d_i=\sum_l(U_{il}^2+V_{il}^2)$, $b^2=\lVert U\rVert^2+\lVert V\rVert^2+2\lVert w\rVert^2=1$ and $g^2=\sum_i(d_i+w_i^2)^2+\sum_{i\ne j}P_{ij}^2Q_{ij}^2$. With $\lambda=\tfrac{3\sqrt2m}{2(2m-1)}$ and $c_0=\tfrac{\sqrt2(2m-1)(4m-1)}{8m^2}$ as in [L278](#lemma-l278-the-odd-mw-for-m-at-least-7-the-walk-inequality-of-j1-on-the-torus-slice-of-so2m1),*
$$\tfrac12\big(\mathop{\mathrm{tr}}P^3+\mathop{\mathrm{tr}}Q^3\big)+3\,w^{\mathsf T}Pw+\lambda g^2\le c_0 .$$

**Proof.** *Seven steps, batch 11 of the working note.* (1) Border $U$ and $V$ by $w/\sqrt2$ on a new index $m+1$ with a zero corner: the bordered pair is symmetric with zero diagonal, of total mass $b^2=1$, and $\mathop{\mathrm{tr}}(\text{bord}\,A)^3=\mathop{\mathrm{tr}}A^3+3\sum_{b,c}z_bA_{bc}z_c$ turns its cubic part into the left side's cubic part plus $3w^{\mathsf T}Pw$, since $Q$ vanishes on the new edges. (2) [L277](#lemma-l277-j1-holds-for-m-at-least-7-so-h-holds-on-the-torus-slice-of-so2m)'s `cocktail_rows` at size $m+1$. (3) Cauchy–Schwarz within the old pairs and within the new ones separately. (4) $\sqrt{FR}\le\tfrac12(tF+R/t)$ at $t_o$ and $t_n$. (5) L277's `pair_bound` on the old pairs, with $(U_{ij}^2-V_{ij}^2)^2=x_{ij}^2(1-\gamma_{ij}^2)=P_{ij}^2Q_{ij}^2$ for $x_{ij}=U_{ij}^2+V_{ij}^2$ and $\gamma_{ij}=2\lvert U_{ij}V_{ij}\rvert/x_{ij}$. (6) The new pairs have $U'=V'$, so their $f'$ and $R'$ are exact: $f'_{i,m+1}=2w_i^2(1-q_{i0})(1-q_{0i})$ and $R_n=2\lVert w\rVert^2\sum_id'_i$. (7) On the walk $w_{ij}=x_{ij}$, $w_{i0}=w_i^2$, whose degrees are $d_i+w_i^2$ and $\lVert w\rVert^2$, the sum of steps 2 to 6 plus $\lambda g^2$ is $L278$'s left side at these $\gamma_{ij}$, so it is at most $c_0$. $\square$

**What it gives.** *J1$_{\rm odd}$ is the odd analogue of L277's J1, and through the mechanism of [L273](#lemma-l273-h-on-the-torus-slice-of-so2m-follows-for-m-at-least-5-from-one-cubic-quartic-matrix-inequality) it gives statement H on the torus slice of $\mathfrak{so}(2m+1)$ for $m\ge7$, whose $F$ is $3\langle A,A_\mu\rangle+\tfrac12(\mathop{\mathrm{tr}}P^3+\mathop{\mathrm{tr}}Q^3)+3w^{\mathsf T}Pw$. That formula is derived as [L280](#lemma-l280-the-cubic-form-on-the-torus-slice-of-so2m1), and the conclusion is [L281](#lemma-l281-h-holds-on-the-torus-slice-of-so2m1-for-m-at-least-7).*

#### Lemma L278, the odd MW' for m at least 7: the walk inequality of J1 on the torus slice of so(2m+1)

*Gate 2 by [r1696](../reviews/REVIEW_r1696.md) and [r1697](../reviews/REVIEW_r1697.md), independently: CONFIRMED, every step, presentational gaps closed in the note; Lean fidelity CONFIRMED by [r1707](../reviews/REVIEW_r1707.md). Derived at the research level in [the R48 working note](../reviews/2026-09-27-R48-torus-families.md), batches 6 to 10. Proved in Lean as `oddMWprime` in [OddMW.lean](../lean/HamiltonsCubic/Results/OddMW.lean), one declaration per step; the one-variable steps are Bernstein certificates in $S$ whose coefficients are polynomials in $m-7$ with non-negative coefficients, generated by [cert_oddmw.py](../scripts/misc/cert_oddmw.py) and checked in the kernel.*

**Statement.** *Let $m\ge7$, $n=m-1$, $\lambda=\tfrac{3\sqrt2m}{2(2m-1)}$, $c_0=\tfrac{\sqrt2(2m-1)(4m-1)}{8m^2}$, $t_o=\tfrac{\sqrt2(2m-1)^2}{4m(2m-3)}$, $t_n=\tfrac{\sqrt2(2m-1)}{4(m-1)}$ and $\kappa_o=(2\lambda-1/t_o)/t_o=\tfrac{4m^2(2m-3)(2m+3)}{(2m-1)^4}$. Let $w$ be a symmetric non-negative matrix on the vertices $0,1,\dots,m$ with zero diagonal and $\sum_{i,j}w_{ij}=1$, $\pi_i=\sum_jw_{ij}$, $q_{ij}=w_{ij}/\pi_i$. Then for all $\gamma_{ij}\in[0,1]$, one per ordered pair of old vertices ($i,j\ge1$),*
$$\sum_{i\ne j\ge1}\Big[\tfrac{t_o}2w_{ij}(1-q_{ij})\big(1+\gamma_{ij}(1-2q_{ij})\big)+\big(\lambda-\tfrac1{2t_o}\big)w_{ij}^2(1-\gamma_{ij}^2)\Big]+2t_n\sum_{i\ge1}w_{i0}(1-q_{i0})(1-q_{0i})+\frac1{2t_o}\sum_{i\ne j\ge1}\pi_i\pi_j+\frac{\pi_0}{t_n}\sum_{i\ge1}\pi_i+\lambda\sum_{i\ge1}\pi_i^2\le c_0,$$
*with equality at the uniform point $w_{ij}=\tfrac1{m^2}$ ($i\ne j\ge1$), $w_{i0}=\tfrac1{2m^2}$.*

**Proof.** *The nine steps are batch 10 of the working note; in outline, with $u_i=w_{i0}$, $r_i=u_i/\pi_i$, $S=\sum_{i\ge1}\pi_i=1-\pi_0$, $s_o=\sum_{i\ge1}\pi_i^2$ and $\beta(\pi)=b_0(1-c'\pi)$ L276's slope at $\kappa_o$.* (1) A weighted AM-GM with $\alpha^2=\tfrac{2m-1}{2m}$ splits each new edge between its old row and row $0$. (2) Row $0$ is L276's R0 on $m$ slots with every $\gamma=1$, at a lowered slope. (3) Each old row is L276's row chord on the scaled simplex $\sum_jq_{ij}=1-r_i$: the two-value slack of R0's and R½'s majorants is non-decreasing in the scale, because their chord slopes on $[0,\tfrac1n]$ decrease, so the scale-$1$ inequality gives every scale. (4) A column Cauchy–Schwarz over the old rows and row $0$, with row $0$'s weight matched to the uniform ratio, leaves $-\tfrac{t_o}2\sum_i\pi_i^2/T_i'$. (5) The $r_i$ enter through $\sum_i\pi_i\tilde\psi(r_i)$ for one cubic $\tilde\psi$, bounded by its tangent at $\bar r=\pi_0/S$ when $\bar r\le r_1=\tfrac{2m^2-3m-1}{2(2m^2-5m+1)}$ and by its chord to $(1,0)$ otherwise. (6) The Cauchy–Schwarz term goes as in L276's $H_c$, with the tangent of $\phi=(s_o-\pi^2)/\beta(\pi)$ at $s_o/S$. (7) What remains is $\Phi(S,s_o)$, (8) non-increasing in $s_o$ on $[S^2/m,S/2]$ (the difference quotient is affine in $s_o$ and non-negative at both ends), and (9) $\Phi(S,S^2/m)\le c_0$ on each branch, the cubic branch's slack being $(2Sm-2m+1)^2Q(S,m)$ over a positive product. $\square$

**What it gives.** *L278 is the last link of [the working note](../reviews/2026-09-27-R48-torus-families.md)'s batch 6 chain: with the cocktail-party rows through the embedding of $\mathfrak{so}(2m+1)$ into $\mathfrak{so}(2m+2)$, the split pairing, the linearisation at $t_o,t_n$ and `L275_interp` on the old edges, it gives J1$_{\rm odd}$ for $m\ge7$, and through [L273](#lemma-l273-h-on-the-torus-slice-of-so2m-follows-for-m-at-least-5-from-one-cubic-quartic-matrix-inequality)'s mechanism statement H on the torus slice of $\mathfrak{so}(2m+1)$, odd $n\ge15$. The first of those links is [L279](#lemma-l279-j1-on-the-torus-slice-of-so2m1-for-m-at-least-7), J1$_{\rm odd}$, proved in prose and in Lean; statement H on the odd slice follows as [L281](#lemma-l281-h-holds-on-the-torus-slice-of-so2m1-for-m-at-least-7), with the slice's cubic form derived as [L280](#lemma-l280-the-cubic-form-on-the-torus-slice-of-so2m1). At $m=6$ ($n=13$) the old rows have five slots, where L276's own chord fails.*

#### Lemma L277, J1 holds for m at least 7, so H holds on the torus slice of so(2m)

*Proved in Lean as `J1_holds` and `H_torus_slice` in [J1.lean](../lean/HamiltonsCubic/Results/J1.lean), J1 being stated exactly as the hypothesis `L273_J1_implies_H` takes. The reduction is the J1 working note's ([batches 3, 7, 9 and 11](../reviews/2026-09-27-I117-J1.md)), now typed step by step. Statement fidelity checked by [r1684](../reviews/REVIEW_r1684.md): CONFIRMED on every claim. At every torus-invariant operator, not only the parametrised `torusOp`, as `H_torus_slice_op` in [TorusLift.lean](../lean/HamiltonsCubic/Results/TorusLift.lean) through L268's `L268_rep`.*

**Statement.** *For $m\ge7$ and real symmetric zero-diagonal $m\times m$ matrices $U,V$ with $\tfrac12(\lVert P\rVert^2+\lVert Q\rVert^2)=1$, $P=U+V$, $Q=U-V$, J1 holds: $\tfrac12(\mathop{\mathrm{tr}}P^3+\mathop{\mathrm{tr}}Q^3)+\lambda_m\big(\sum_id_i^2+\sum_{i\ne j}P_{ij}^2Q_{ij}^2\big)\le\lambda_m$. Hence ([L273](#lemma-l273-h-on-the-torus-slice-of-so2m-follows-for-m-at-least-5-from-one-cubic-quartic-matrix-inequality) (3)) $F(C)\le\kappa\lVert C\rVert^3$ for every torus-invariant $C\in\mathop{\mathrm{Sym}}^2\mathfrak{so}(2m)$, $\kappa=2(m-1)/\sqrt{m(2m-1)}$.*

**Proof.** *(1) Cocktail-party rows (`cocktail_rows`): $\tfrac12(\mathop{\mathrm{tr}}P^3+\mathop{\mathrm{tr}}Q^3)=\sum_{i,j}\big(U_{ij}\alpha_{ij}+V_{ij}\beta_{ij}\big)$ with $\alpha_{ij}=\sum_k(U_{ik}U_{kj}+V_{ik}V_{kj})$, $\beta_{ij}=\sum_k(U_{ik}V_{kj}+V_{ik}U_{kj})$, and Cauchy–Schwarz over $k\ne i,j$ bounds both by $\sqrt{(d_i-w_{ij})(d_j-w_{ij})}$, $w_{ij}=U_{ij}^2+V_{ij}^2$. (2) Pairing (`pairing`) against $R_{ij}=d_id_j-(U_{ij}^2-V_{ij}^2)^2$, which exceeds $(d_i-w)(d_j-w)$ by $w(d_i-w)+w(d_j-w)+4U_{ij}^2V_{ij}^2$, with $\sum R=1-g^2$. (3) Per pair (`pair_bound`), with $p=w/d_i$, $q=w/d_j$, $\gamma=2\lvert U_{ij}V_{ij}\rvert/w$: $(\lvert U\rvert+\lvert V\rvert)^2=w(1+\gamma)$ and $R=d_id_j(1-pq(1-\gamma^2))$, so `L275_interp` bounds the ratio by $w\big[\gamma((1-p)^2+(1-q)^2)+(1-\gamma)\tfrac{(1-p)+(1-q)}2\big]$. (4) Summed and symmetrised this is MW's left side, and [L276](#lemma-l276-mw-for-m-at-least-7-the-decoupled-walk-inequality-of-j1) bounds it by $\kappa'\sum R$, $\kappa'=\lambda_m^2$ (`J1b`). (5) Hence $T\le\sqrt{\kappa'\sum R}\sqrt{\sum R}=\lambda_m(1-g^2)$.* $\square$

**What it gives.** *H on the torus slice at $2m\ge14$ in the kernel, and U$_{\mathrm s}'$ on the torus family from it in one line of prose: at an idempotent $F(C)=\lVert C\rVert^2$, so $\lVert C\rVert\ge1/\kappa$, which is [R47](../routes.md) for $m\ge7$. At $m=6$ J1 holds with one step resting on an exact certificate ([working note](../reviews/2026-09-27-I117-J1.md), batch 13), outside this lemma.*

#### Lemma L276, MW' for m at least 7: the decoupled walk inequality of J1

*Gate 2 by [r1671](../reviews/REVIEW_r1671.md) and [r1672](../reviews/REVIEW_r1672.md), independently: CONFIRMED, every step, presentational gaps closed. Derived at the research level in [the J1 working note](../reviews/2026-09-27-I117-J1.md), batch 11. Proved in Lean as `MWprime` in [MWPrime.lean](../lean/HamiltonsCubic/Results/MWPrime.lean), one declaration per step: `row_chord` (from `R0` and `Rhalf`), `left_concave`, `L1_nonpos`, `L2_nonneg`, `Hc`.*

**Statement.** *Let $m\ge7$, $n=m-1$, $\kappa=\lambda_m^2=2(n+1)(n-1)^2/n^3$, and let $w$ be a symmetric non-negative $m\times m$ matrix with zero diagonal and $\sum_{i,j}w_{ij}=1$, with $\pi_i=\sum_jw_{ij}$, $q_{ij}=w_{ij}/\pi_i$ and $s=\sum_i\pi_i^2$. Then for every $\gamma_{ij}\in[0,1]$, one per ordered pair,*
$$\sum_{i,j}\Big[w_{ij}(1-q_{ij})\big(1+\gamma_{ij}(1-2q_{ij})\big)+\kappa w_{ij}^2(1-\gamma_{ij}^2)\Big]\le\kappa(1-s),$$
*equivalently $\sum_i\pi_i\sum_jF(q_{ij},\pi_i)\le\kappa(1-s)$ with $F(q,\pi)=\max_{\gamma\in[0,1]}\big[q(1-q)(1+\gamma(1-2q))+\kappa\pi q^2(1-\gamma^2)\big]$. Equality holds at the uniform point and at the edge.*

**Proof.** *(1) Row chord. For $\pi\in[0,\tfrac12]$ and a probability vector $q$ on $n$ points, $\sum_jF(q_j,\pi)\le\kappa\pi+\beta(\pi)(1-\sum_jq_j^2)$ with $\beta(\pi)=\tfrac{2(n-1)}n-\tfrac{n\kappa\pi}{n-1}$, fixed by the uniform row and a point mass. With $\gamma$ explicit both sides are affine in $\pi$, so it suffices at $\pi=0$ and $\pi=\tfrac12$. At $\pi=0$ it is Lemma A with the entries above $\tfrac12$ taken at $\gamma=0$. At $\pi=\tfrac12$ the per-entry maximum $\psi$ is concave on $[0,\tfrac1n]$ (entries up to $\tfrac7{32}$ are light), and the equal-variable reduction (Cirtoaje's right-convex-function theorem reflected) leaves one large entry $x$ and $n-1$ equal ones, where $\psi(x)+(n-1)\psi(\tfrac{1-x}{n-1})$ is $2(x-c)^2\big((x-r)+\tfrac{y-r}{n-1}\big)\le0$ on $[\tfrac1n,\tfrac7{32}]$, $(x-1)L_2/(4n^3(n-1)^2(n+1))\le0$ on $[\tfrac7{32},\tfrac12]$, and $(1-x)L_1/(n^3(n-1)^2)\le0$ on $[\tfrac12,1]$, with $L_2\ge0$ by its Bernstein coefficients (polynomials in $n-6$ with positive coefficients) and $L_1\le0$ a convex quadratic negative at both ends for $n\ge6$. (2) Every $\pi_i\le\tfrac12$, $\beta>0$, and Cauchy–Schwarz over each column gives $\sum_i\beta_i\pi_i\sum_jq_{ij}^2\ge\sum_j\pi_j^2/T_j$, $T_j=\sum_{i\ne j}\pi_i/\beta_i$. (3) With $c'=\tfrac{n+1}n$, $g_i=\pi_i/(1-c'\pi_i)$, $B=\sum_ig_i$, what remains is $H_c$: $\sum_j\pi_j^2/(B-g_j)\ge(1+(n+1)(n-2)s)/n^2$. Titu's form reduces it to $\sum_i\pi_i\phi(\pi_i)$ with $\phi=(s-\pi^2)/(1-c'\pi)$ concave ($c'^2s<1$), its tangent at $\pi=s$ bounds that by $s(1-s)/(1-c's)$, and what is left is $(1-2s)((n+1)s-1)\ge0$.* $\square$

**What it gives.** *MW' implies MW (the symmetric $\gamma_{ij}=\gamma_{ji}$), and with the per-pair interpolation `L275_interp` MW gives J1*b, which gives J1 through the cocktail-party row bound and the pairing with $R_{ij}=d_id_j-P_{ij}^2Q_{ij}^2$ ([working note](../reviews/2026-09-27-I117-J1.md), batches 3, 7 and 9). So J1 holds on the whole torus slice for $m\ge7$, and by [L273](#lemma-l273-h-on-the-torus-slice-of-so2m-follows-for-m-at-least-5-from-one-cubic-quartic-matrix-inequality) (3), whose proof uses J1 only at the point itself, statement H holds at every torus-invariant point of $\mathop{\mathrm{Sym}}^2\mathfrak{so}(2m)$ for $m\ge7$. The reduction from J1 to MW' is proved in Lean too, as [L277](#lemma-l277-j1-holds-for-m-at-least-7-so-h-holds-on-the-torus-slice-of-so2m). At $m=6$ ($2m=12$) the point-mass slope $\tfrac32-\kappa\pi$ exceeds $\beta$ for $\pi>\tfrac{25}{96}$ and the row chord fails, which is the open case.*

#### Lemma L273, H on the torus slice of so(2m) follows for m at least 5 from one cubic-quartic matrix inequality

*Gate 2 by [r1593](../reviews/REVIEW_r1593.md) and [r1594](../reviews/REVIEW_r1594.md), independently: CONFIRMED with minor gaps, r1593 PLAUSIBLE on *What it gives*; repaired: the equality case in (1), the Lagrange sentence's positivity condition, the edge equality marked as screened, the threshold sentence narrowed, a docstring label. Derived at the research level in [the D6 working note](../reviews/2026-09-26-I73-class-ceiling.md), batches 51 and 52. Typed in [I117LemmaF.lean](../lean/HamiltonsCubic/Results/I117LemmaF.lean) (`jh`, `jh_mono`, `jh_threshold`, `jh_zero`, `jh_constants`, `jh_nonneg`, the last being (2) in the variable $t$ with the actual constants); (1), the substitution $t=\sqrt{1+a^2}$ in (2), the failure at $m=4$ and the implication in (3) are prose. At the operator level (1), (2) and (3) are proved (`L273_F_bound`, `psi_bound`, `L273_J1_implies_H`, [r1631](../reviews/REVIEW_r1631.md)), (2) through a tangent-line argument in place of the minimisation, and (1) and (3) at every torus-invariant operator, not only at the parametrised `torusOp`, as `L273_F_bound_op` and `L273_J1_op` in [L273Op.lean](../lean/HamiltonsCubic/Results/L273Op.lean) through L268's representation `L268_rep`, the dependency r1631 recorded, with (1)'s equality case and $g\ge1/\sqrt m$ as `L273_F_eq_op`, `L273_g_ge_op` ([r1755](../reviews/REVIEW_r1755.md)); (2)'s equality at $g=1/\sqrt m$ and the failure at $m=4$ are prose.*

**Statement.** *In [L271](#lemma-l271-the-torus-system-is-two-coupled-copies-of-l261s-exchanged-by-p-and-q)'s coordinates let $X=(A,P,Q)$ be torus-invariant with $b^2=\tfrac12(\lVert P\rVert^2+\lVert Q\rVert^2)=1$, $g=\lVert A_\mu\rVert$ and $T=\tfrac12(\mathop{\mathrm{tr}}P^3+\mathop{\mathrm{tr}}Q^3)$, and put $\kappa=c/\sqrt N=\tfrac{2(m-1)}{\sqrt{m(2m-1)}}$, $\lambda_m=\tfrac{\sqrt{2m}\,(m-2)}{(m-1)^{3/2}}$ and $\Psi_\kappa(g)=\min_{a\ge0}\big(\kappa(1+a^2)^{3/2}-3ag\big)$. (1) $F(X)\le3\lVert A\rVert g+T$, with equality when $A$ is a non-negative multiple of $A_\mu$, and $g\ge1/\sqrt m$. (2) For $m\ge5$, $\lambda_m(1-g^2)\le\Psi_\kappa(g)$ for every $g\ge1/\sqrt m$, with equality at $g=1/\sqrt m$; at $m=4$ it fails for $g$ just above $1/\sqrt m$. (3) Hence for $m\ge5$,*
$$\textbf{(J1)}\qquad T+\lambda_m\,g^2\le\lambda_m\quad\text{whenever }b=1$$
*implies H on the torus slice: $F(X)\le\kappa\lVert X\rVert^3$ for every torus-invariant $X\in\mathop{\mathrm{Sym}}^2\mathfrak{so}(2m)$.*

**Proof.** *(1) [L271](#lemma-l271-the-torus-system-is-two-coupled-copies-of-l261s-exchanged-by-p-and-q) (1) is $F=3\langle A,A_\mu\rangle+T$ and Cauchy–Schwarz bounds the first term; $\mathop{\mathrm{tr}}A_\mu=\sum_id_i=b^2=1$ gives $g\ge1/\sqrt m$. (2) The minimum defining $\Psi_\kappa$ sits at $\kappa a\sqrt{1+a^2}=g$, increasing in $a$; with $t=\sqrt{1+a^2}$, $\Psi_\kappa(g)=\kappa t(3-2t^2)$ and $g^2=\kappa^2t^2(t^2-1)$, so $\Psi_\kappa(g)-\lambda_m(1-g^2)=h(t):=\lambda_m\kappa^2t^4-2\kappa t^3-\lambda_m\kappa^2t^2+3\kappa t-\lambda_m$. At $t_0^2=\tfrac{2m-1}{2(m-1)}$, $g^2=\tfrac1m$ and $\kappa t_0(3-2t_0^2)=\tfrac{\sqrt2(m-2)}{\sqrt{m(m-1)}}=\lambda_m(1-\tfrac1m)$, so $h(t_0)=0$ (`jh_zero`). The Taylor coefficients of $h$ at $t_0$ are $(2t_0^2-1)\kappa(2\lambda_m\kappa t_0-3)$, $\kappa(6\lambda_m\kappa t_0^2-6t_0-\lambda_m\kappa)$, $2\kappa(2\lambda_m\kappa t_0-1)$ and $\lambda_m\kappa^2$, all non-negative once $t_0^2\ge\tfrac12$ and $2\lambda_m\kappa t_0\ge3$ (`jh_mono`); and $\lambda_m\kappa t_0=\tfrac{2(m-2)}{m-1}$, so the second condition is $m\ge5$ (`jh_threshold`). At $m=4$ the first coefficient is negative. (3) By homogeneity take $b=1$ ($b=0$ gives $F=0$, $A\#A$ vanishing on the Cartan). By (1) and J1, $F\le3ag+\lambda_m(1-g^2)$ with $a=\lVert A\rVert$, and by (2) this is at most $3ag+\Psi_\kappa(g)\le\kappa(1+a^2)^{3/2}=\kappa\lVert X\rVert^3$.* $\square$

**What it gives.** *[R47](../routes.md)'s strongest torus form rests on J1, a quartic inequality in the root weights alone, with equality at the uniform point and at the $\Lambda^2_+$ edge ($P_{12}=\pm Q_{12}$ the only non-zero entries), with their images under $P\leftrightarrow Q$, $Q\mapsto SQS$ and index permutations the equality cases the screens find, and (2) is where this route's threshold $m\ge5$ enters: $4(m-2)\ge3(m-1)$. On the $m=4$ slice H itself fails globally, at the torus-invariant $\Lambda^2_+$ point with $F/\lVert X\rVert^3=2/\sqrt3>\kappa_4$, while the uniform point stays a strict local maximum there (a Hessian screen, [r1593](../reviews/REVIEW_r1593.md)). Screens: J1 at $m=4,5,6,8$ (120 BFGS starts each) reaches $\lambda_m$ and never exceeds it ([screen_I117_J1_linecap.py](../scripts/misc/screen_I117_J1_linecap.py), [batch 52](../reviews/2026-09-26-I73-class-ceiling.md)); at every critical point of $T+\lambda_mg^2$ on the root sphere whose multiplier $\nu=\tfrac12(3T+4\lambda_mg^2)$ is positive (every maximiser), the Lagrange equations are, after rescaling $(P,Q)$, $(P^2)_{ij}=\theta P_{ij}(1-d_i-d_j-2Q_{ij}^2)$ and the same with $P,Q$ exchanged, for one $\theta>0$, a one-parameter deformation of L271 (2)'s system ($\theta=1$) whose value at each point decides J1, so a proof must be global.
