J1 at $Q=0$ is a stability form of the traceless cubic bound: the deficit $T_u-T$ dominates $\lambda_m$ times the excess $g^2-\tfrac1m$ of the degree concentration.*

#### Lemma L272, the Perron root of P plus diag d bounds every Cartan weight below, and the root weights sum to at most m/2

*Gate 2 by [r1589](../reviews/REVIEW_r1589.md) and [r1590](../reviews/REVIEW_r1590.md), independently: CONFIRMED, the screen range widened to L267's $\rho=\tfrac{13}{12}$ at $m=4$ and the eigenvalue count sharpened. Derived at the research level in [the D6 working note](../reviews/2026-09-26-I73-class-ceiling.md), batch 49. Typed in [I117LemmaF.lean](../lean/HamiltonsCubic/Results/I117LemmaF.lean) (`perron_row`, `perron_row_eq`, `perron_d`, `perron_d_eq`, `perron_x`) and proved at the operator level in [I117Statements.lean](../lean/HamiltonsCubic/Results/I117Statements.lean): (1) as `L272_square`, (2) and the bound in (3) as `L272_perron`, the vanishing in (4) as `L272_support`, and the positive Perron vector as `perron_positive_eigvec` (a maximiser of the quadratic form on the unit sphere, with `psd_kernel`); (3)'s equality case and (4)'s eigenvalue count are prose.*

**Statement.** *In [L271](#lemma-l271-the-torus-system-is-two-coupled-copies-of-l261s-exchanged-by-p-and-q)'s notation, at a positive solution of the torus system let $X=P+\mathop{\mathrm{diag}}d$, $\pi_i=\sum_lP_{il}^2$, $\rho$ the largest eigenvalue of $X$, and $S=\{i:Q_{il}\ne0\text{ for some }l\}$. (1) Off the diagonal $X^2=X-2P\circ Q\circ Q$, and $(X^2-X)_{ii}=\pi_i+d_i^2-d_i$. (2) $d_i\ge\rho-1$ for every $i$, and $d_i=\rho-1$ for every $i\notin S$. (3) $x=\sum_\alpha\mu_\alpha\le\tfrac m2$, the value at $I/c$, with equality only at $I/c$. (4) $(X-\rho)(X+\rho-1)$ vanishes outside $S\times S$, so at least $m-\lvert S\rvert$ eigenvalues of $X$, counted with multiplicity, lie in $\{\rho,1-\rho\}$, and since $\rho$ is simple at least $m-\lvert S\rvert-1$ of them equal $1-\rho$.*

**Proof.** *(1) L271's $P$ equation is $(P^2)_{ij}+P_{ij}(d_i+d_j)=P_{ij}-2P_{ij}Q_{ij}^2$ for $i\ne j$, and $(X^2)_{ii}=\pi_i+d_i^2$. (2) $X$ is non-negative with positive off-diagonal entries, so Perron–Frobenius gives $w>0$ with $Xw=\rho w$ and $\rho>0$. By (1) and $P\circ Q\circ Q\ge0$ ($P>0$), $X^2\le X+\mathop{\mathrm{diag}}(\pi_i+d_i^2-d_i)$ entrywise, so $\rho^2-\rho\le\pi_i+d_i^2-d_i$ at every row (`perron_row`); $\pi_i\le\pi_i+\sum_lQ_{il}^2=2d_i$ gives $\rho^2-\rho\le d_i^2+d_i$, hence $d_i\ge\rho-1$ (`perron_d`). For $i\notin S$ row $i$ of the bound is an equality and $\pi_i=2d_i$, so $\rho^2-\rho=d_i^2+d_i$ (`perron_row_eq`) and $d_i=\rho-1$ since $d_i+\rho>0$ (`perron_d_eq`). (3) Summing (2), $\sum_id_i\ge m(\rho-1)$; the Rayleigh quotient at the all-ones vector gives $m\rho\ge\mathbf 1^{\mathsf T}X\mathbf 1=\sum_id_i+2x$, so $2x\le m$ (`perron_x`). Equality makes every $d_i=\rho-1$, then $\rho^2-\rho\le\pi_i+d_i^2-d_i$ forces $\pi_i=2d_i$, so $Q=0$ and L271 (3) gives $I/c$. (4) The diagonal of $X^2-X-(\rho^2-\rho)I$ is $\pi_i+d_i^2-d_i-(\rho^2-\rho)$, zero for $i\notin S$ by (2), and its off-diagonal is $-2P_{ij}Q_{ij}^2$, zero unless $i,j\in S$.* $\square$

**What it gives.** *(3) is the root-weight companion of [L260](#lemma-l260-statement-h-reduces-to-the-least-norm-of-a-positive-definite--idempotent-on-a-semisimple-subalgebra) (4)'s $c\tau\le d$, and like it bounds from above. With (2), $q=\sum_id_i^2+\sum_id_i+\sum_{i\ne j}P_{ij}^2Q_{ij}^2\ge m(\rho^2-\rho)$, which reaches $I/c$'s value $m(2m-1)/c^2$ exactly when $\rho\ge1+\tfrac1c$. Screens at $m=4,5,6$: $\rho<1+\tfrac1c$ at every non-uniform solution computed ($1.006$ to $1.083$, the top being L267's $\rho=\tfrac{13}{12}$ at $m=4$, where $d_i=4b^2+2a_s^2=\tfrac1{12}$ off $S$, against $1.10$ to $1.17$), and $\min_id_i=\rho-1$ there, so their heaviness sits in the $d_i$ with $i\in S$, which (2) bounds only from below. The light side of [L270](#lemma-l270-the-direction-i-destabilises-only-idempotents-heavier-than-ic-and-h-asks-only-that-stable-idempotents-are-not-lighter) on the torus family needs control of $d$ on $S$.*

#### Lemma L271, the torus system is two coupled copies of L261's, exchanged by P and Q

*Gate 2 by [r1586](../reviews/REVIEW_r1586.md) and [r1587](../reviews/REVIEW_r1587.md), independently: CONFIRMED, (4) restricted to $Q\ne0$, the positive-definiteness clause restricted to idempotents, and the screen list completed; the slice-eigenvalue range in *What it gives* is screened by r1586 and left unchecked by r1587 (PLAUSIBLE there). Derived at the research level in [the D6 working note](../reviews/2026-09-26-I73-class-ceiling.md), batch 48. The scalar and matrix cores are typed in [I117LemmaF.lean](../lean/HamiltonsCubic/Results/I117LemmaF.lean) (`pq_entry`, `pq_square`, `pq_triangles`, `qsplit_test`); (2)'s symmetries and positivity criterion are typed in [L271Sym.lean](../lean/HamiltonsCubic/Results/L271Sym.lean) ($P\leftrightarrow Q$ in `PQSys_swap`, `Fpq_swap`; $Q\mapsto SQS$, $A\mapsto SAS$ in `PQSys_sign`, `Fpq_sign`; the $(A_{\mathrm{off}},Q)$ sign flip changing $F$ by $-\mathop{\mathrm{tr}}Q^3$ in `Fpq_neg`; $U,V>0$ exactly when $P>\lvert Q\rvert$ in `pos_iff`); (3) at the operator is `L271_Q_zero_op` (a positive-definite idempotent `torusOp A U U` equals $I/(2(m-1))$) and `L271_other_idem` (every other torus-invariant self-adjoint positive-definite idempotent has $U_{ij}\ne V_{ij}$ on some pair), in [L268Rep.lean](../lean/HamiltonsCubic/Results/L268Rep.lean), which also supplies (4)'s $Q\ne0$ at $C\ne I/c$, and (4)'s hypotheses for `L271_eigen` are one theorem, `L271_four_pos` ([r1754](../reviews/REVIEW_r1754.md)); the reduction of $F$ to $(A,P,Q)$ is prose; part (3) is `L271_Q_zero` and (4)'s polarisation `L271_split_core` in I117Statements.lean, and (4)'s eigenvalue and threshold are `L271_eigen`, `L271_threshold` in [L271Eigen.lean](../lean/HamiltonsCubic/Results/L271Eigen.lean) ([r1734](../reviews/REVIEW_r1734.md)). At the operator level (1) is `L271_F_slice` and (4)'s identity `L271_split`, both proved ([r1629](../reviews/REVIEW_r1629.md), [r1631](../reviews/REVIEW_r1631.md)).*

**Statement.** *In [L268](#lemma-l268-the-torus-invariant-idempotents-of-so2m-are-the-positive-solutions-of-one-cubic-system-in-the-root-weights)'s notation write $U_{ij}=\mu_{\varepsilon_i-\varepsilon_j}$ and $V_{ij}=\mu_{\varepsilon_i+\varepsilon_j}$ for $i\ne j$ (symmetric $m\times m$ matrices with zero diagonal), $P=U+V$, $Q=U-V$ and $d_i=\sum_l(U_{il}^2+V_{il}^2)$. (1) Every torus-invariant $C=A\oplus\bigoplus_\alpha\mu_\alpha I_{R_\alpha}$ has $\lVert C\rVert^2=\lVert A\rVert^2+\tfrac12\lVert P\rVert^2+\tfrac12\lVert Q\rVert^2$ and*
$$F(C,C,C)=3\sum_iA_{ii}d_i-3\sum_{i\ne j}A_{ij}P_{ij}Q_{ij}+\tfrac12\mathop{\mathrm{tr}}P^3+\tfrac12\mathop{\mathrm{tr}}Q^3.$$
*(2) $C$ is an idempotent exactly when $A_{ii}=d_i$, $A_{ij}=-P_{ij}Q_{ij}$ for $i\ne j$, and for every $i\ne j$*
$$P_{ij}\big(1-d_i-d_j-2Q_{ij}^2\big)=(P^2)_{ij},\qquad Q_{ij}\big(1-d_i-d_j-2P_{ij}^2\big)=(Q^2)_{ij}.$$
*The system and $F$ are invariant under $P\leftrightarrow Q$ and under $Q\mapsto SQS$, $A\mapsto SAS$ for a diagonal sign matrix $S$, and $(A_{\mathrm{off}},Q)\mapsto-(A_{\mathrm{off}},Q)$ fixes every term of $F$ except $\tfrac12\mathop{\mathrm{tr}}Q^3$. At an idempotent, positive definiteness is $P_{ij}>\lvert Q_{ij}\rvert$ (the Cartan block $A=A_\mu$ is then positive definite by L268), which $P\leftrightarrow Q$ does not preserve. (3) An idempotent in the family has $Q=0$ exactly when it is coordinate-diagonal, so by [L261](#lemma-l261-u-holds-on-the-coordinate-diagonal-family-the-only-positive-definite-coordinate-diagonal--idempotent-is-in-2) the only positive solution with $Q=0$ is $I/c$, and every other torus-invariant positive-definite idempotent has $\mu_{\varepsilon_i+\varepsilon_j}\ne\mu_{\varepsilon_i-\varepsilon_j}$ for some pair. (4) For $C\ne I/c$ (so $Q\ne0$ by (3)), with $C_P=(\mathop{\mathrm{diag}}d,\,U=V=\tfrac12P)$ and $C_Q=(A_{\mathrm{off}},\,U=-V=\tfrac12Q)$, orthogonal with sum $C$, the second eigenvalue of $C\#\cdot$ on $\mathop{\mathrm{span}}(C_P,C_Q)$ is*
$$\tfrac12-\frac{\lVert C_Q\rVert^2}{2\lVert C_P\rVert^2}+\frac{q\mathop{\mathrm{tr}}Q^3}{4\lVert C_P\rVert^2\lVert C_Q\rVert^2},\qquad q=\lVert C\rVert^2,$$
*above $\tfrac12$ exactly when $q\mathop{\mathrm{tr}}Q^3>2\lVert C_Q\rVert^4$.*

**Proof.** *(1) From L268's proof, $(C\#C)_{\mathfrak t}=A_\mu:=2\sum_\alpha\mu_\alpha^2u_\alpha u_\alpha^{\mathsf T}$ for every $A$ and $(C\#C)_\alpha=2\mu_\alpha u_\alpha^{\mathsf T}Au_\alpha+S_\alpha$ with $S_\alpha$ the pair sum, so $F=\langle A,A_\mu\rangle+2\sum_\alpha\mu_\alpha(2\mu_\alpha u_\alpha^{\mathsf T}Au_\alpha+S_\alpha)=3\langle A,A_\mu\rangle+6T_3$, $T_3$ the sum of $\mu_\alpha\mu_\beta\mu_\gamma$ over triangles. $(A_\mu)_{ii}=d_i$ and $(A_\mu)_{ij}=V_{ij}^2-U_{ij}^2=-P_{ij}Q_{ij}$. The triangles on $\{i,j,k\}$ are the four sign patterns with an even number of roots $\varepsilon_a+\varepsilon_b$, so $P_{ij}P_{jk}P_{ik}+Q_{ij}Q_{jk}Q_{ik}$ is twice their sum (`pq_triangles`) and $\mathop{\mathrm{tr}}P^3+\mathop{\mathrm{tr}}Q^3=12T_3$. (2) The pair sums are $S_{\varepsilon_i-\varepsilon_j}=(U^2+V^2)_{ij}$ and $S_{\varepsilon_i+\varepsilon_j}=(UV+VU)_{ij}$, whose sum and difference are $(P^2)_{ij}$ and $(Q^2)_{ij}$ (`pq_square`), and $u_\alpha^{\mathsf T}Au_\alpha=\tfrac12(d_i+d_j)\mp A_{ij}$ for $\alpha=\varepsilon_i\mp\varepsilon_j$; with $A=A_\mu$ the sum and difference of L268's two equations for the pair are the stated pair (`pq_entry`). (3) The block $\mathop{\mathrm{span}}\{e_a\wedge e_b:a\in\{2i-1,2i\},\,b\in\{2j-1,2j\}\}$ is $R_{\varepsilon_i+\varepsilon_j}\oplus R_{\varepsilon_i-\varepsilon_j}$, on which $C$ has the off-diagonal coordinate entries $\pm\tfrac12(U_{ij}-V_{ij})$; with $Q=0$, $A=\mathop{\mathrm{diag}}d$ in the basis $e_{2i-1}\wedge e_{2i}$, and every coordinate weight is positive. (4) Polarising (1) gives $F(C,C_P,C_Q)=\sum_{i\ne j}P_{ij}^2Q_{ij}^2+\tfrac14\sum_{i\ne j}(d_i+d_j)Q_{ij}^2$; contracting the $Q$ equation with $Q$ gives $\sum_{i\ne j}Q_{ij}^2(1-d_i-d_j-2P_{ij}^2)=\mathop{\mathrm{tr}}Q^3$, so $F(C,C_P,C_Q)=\tfrac12\lVert C_Q\rVert^2-\tfrac14\mathop{\mathrm{tr}}Q^3$. $C\#C=C$ makes the rows of $L_{kl}=F(C,C_k,C_l)$ sum to $G=\mathop{\mathrm{diag}}(\lVert C_P\rVert^2,\lVert C_Q\rVert^2)$, so the second eigenvalue is $1-L_{PQ}(1/G_{PP}+1/G_{QQ})$ (`qsplit_test`).* $\square$

**What it gives.** *The torus system is L261's coordinate-diagonal system (the fixed set $Q=0$) and its mirror, coupled through $d$ and the terms $2P_{ij}^2$, $2Q_{ij}^2$, and every non-uniform solution breaks the grading that only $\tfrac12\mathop{\mathrm{tr}}Q^3$ breaks in $F$. Screens at $m=4,5$: at every positive solution found $Q$ is supported on the edges among two or three indices, one edge at [L267](#lemma-l267-u-fails-on-son-itself-for-every-n-at-least-7-unstably-and-heavier-than-in-2)'s ($\mathop{\mathrm{tr}}Q^3=0$) and a triangle with positive sign product at the $\mathfrak{su}(3)$-seeded ones; (4)'s eigenvalue there runs from $0.35$ to $0.43$ ($0.38$ at L267's for $m=4$), while the whole torus slice carries an eigenvalue $0.55$ to $0.63$ on $C^\perp$ at each.*

#### Lemma L270, the direction I destabilises only idempotents heavier than I/c, and H asks only that stable idempotents are not lighter

*Gate 2 by [r1586](../reviews/REVIEW_r1586.md) and [r1587](../reviews/REVIEW_r1587.md), independently: CONFIRMED, the reverse inequality in (3) written in and one sentence of *What it gives* narrowed to the stable idempotents. Derived at the research level in [the D6 working note](../reviews/2026-09-26-I73-class-ceiling.md), batch 48. Typed in [I117LemmaF.lean](../lean/HamiltonsCubic/Results/I117LemmaF.lean) (`idir_test`, `idir_heavy`, `torus_light_y`, `l267_idir_n9`) and proved at the operator level in [I117Statements.lean](../lean/HamiltonsCubic/Results/I117Statements.lean): (1)–(2) as `L270_so`, (4) as `L270_torus_light`, and at every torus-invariant self-adjoint positive-definite idempotent of $\mathfrak{so}(2m)$ lighter than $I/(2(m-1))$ as `L270_light_op` in [L270Op.lean](../lean/HamiltonsCubic/Results/L270Op.lean) through `L268_full`, and (3)'s reduction as `L270_H_of_Us'`, and to simple subalgebras as `L270_H_of_Us_simple` in [L270Simple.lean](../lean/HamiltonsCubic/Results/L270Simple.lean) (H at $n\ge9$ from $\lVert C\rVert^2\ge N/(n-2)^2$ at the stable positive-semidefinite idempotents with simple range); and the converse, H at $n\ge9$ equivalent to that bound, as `L270_H_iff_Us_simple`, since $F(C,C,C)=\lVert C\rVert^2$ at every self-adjoint idempotent, so the reduction locates where a counterexample to H must live without weakening what is to be proved; what stays prose is the comparison $d/c^2\ge N/(n-2)^2$, from [L256](#lemma-l256-the-internal-mass-cap-on-subalgebras-a-closed-e_0oplus-e_2-cannot-reach-theta_0) together with $\mathcal M(\mathfrak h)=2c_{\mathfrak h}d$ ([r1767](../reviews/REVIEW_r1767.md)).*

**Statement.** *Let $\mathfrak h$ be compact simple with $I\#I=cI$ and $d=\dim\mathfrak h$, and let $C\ne I/c$ be a positive-definite $\#$-idempotent on $\mathfrak h$ with $\tau=\mathop{\mathrm{tr}}C$ and $q=\lVert C\rVert^2$. (1) On $\mathop{\mathrm{span}}\{C,I\}$ the second eigenvalue of $C\#\cdot$ is*
$$\lambda_I=\frac{cq\tau-\tau^2}{qd-\tau^2},$$
*on the projection of $I$ onto $C^\perp$, and $\lambda_I>\tfrac12$ exactly when $q(2c\tau-d)>\tau^2$. (2) That condition forces $q>\tau^2/(2c\tau-d)\ge d/c^2$: the direction $I$ destabilises only idempotents strictly heavier than $I/c$, and $\lambda_I\le\tfrac12$ at every positive-definite idempotent with $q\le d/c^2$. L264 (4)'s two-component criterion is (1) on $\mathop{\mathrm{span}}(P_{\mathfrak k},P_{\mathfrak p})=\mathop{\mathrm{span}}\{C,I\}$, so every unstable second root there is heavier than $I/c$. (3) Statement H at $n\ge9$ follows from* **U$_{\mathrm s}'$**: *every stable positive-definite idempotent on a compact simple $\mathfrak h$ has $\lVert C\rVert^2\ge d/c^2$. U$_{\mathrm s}$ implies it, and it asks nothing of idempotents heavier than $I/c$. (4) On the torus family of L268, a positive-definite idempotent lighter than $I/c$ has $\sum_\alpha\mu_\alpha^2<\tfrac m{4(m-1)}$, the value at $I/c$.*

**Proof.** *(1) $B(X,Y)=\langle C\#X,Y\rangle$ is symmetric, $C\#C=C$ gives $B(C,Y)=\langle C,Y\rangle$, and $\langle C\#I,I\rangle=\langle C,I\#I\rangle=c\tau$. For $H=I-(\tau/q)C$, $\langle H,C\rangle=0$, so $B(C,H)=0$ and $\{C,H\}$ diagonalises $B$ against the metric on the plane; $B(H,H)=c\tau-\tau^2/q$ and $\lVert H\rVert^2=d-\tau^2/q>0$ by strict Cauchy–Schwarz, $C$ not being a multiple of $I$. Multiplying $2B(H,H)>\lVert H\rVert^2$ by $q$ gives $q(2c\tau-d)>\tau^2$ (`idir_test`). (2) The left side is positive only when $2c\tau>d$, and $c^2\tau^2-d(2c\tau-d)=(c\tau-d)^2\ge0$ (`idir_heavy`). In L264 (4), $C=aP_{\mathfrak k}+bP_{\mathfrak p}$ with $a\ne b$ spans the plane with $I$. (3) By L260 (1)–(3) and L264 (1)–(2) the maximiser of $F$ on the unit sphere gives a stable positive-definite idempotent $C$ on a simple $\mathfrak h\subseteq\mathfrak{so}(n)$ with $\lVert C\rVert^2=1/\kappa_n^2$; U$_{\mathrm s}'$ gives $\lVert C\rVert^2\ge d/c_{\mathfrak h}^2$ and L256 gives $d/c_{\mathfrak h}^2\ge N/(n-2)^2$, so $\kappa_n\le(n-2)/\sqrt N$; the reverse inequality is the value at $I$ ([L259](#lemma-l259-the-quartic-bound-the-internal-cap-and-the-symmetric-family-are-one-norm-statement-for-hamiltons--proved-outside-a-trace-window-of-idempotents) (2)), so equality, which is H. (4) $q=\lVert A\rVert^2+2y\ge(\mathop{\mathrm{tr}}A)^2/m+2y=4y^2/m+2y$ with $y=\sum_\alpha\mu_\alpha^2$ and $\mathop{\mathrm{tr}}A=2y$, with equality at $I/c$ where $A$ is scalar, and $4y^2/m+2y$ increases on $y\ge0$ (`torus_light_y`).* $\square$

**What it gives.** *[R44](../routes.md)'s target is U$_{\mathrm s}'$, and the direction $I$, along which L264 (4) reads its instabilities, is blind to it. The test holds at L267's idempotent (exactly $(39350\sqrt{13}-122242)/8192>0$ at $n=9$, `l267_idir_n9`), at both $\mathfrak g_2$ idempotents through L264 (4), and at every torus solution computed at $m=4,5$, and each of these is heavier than $I/c$. Every idempotent computed other than $I/c$ is therefore harmless to H by weight alone, whatever its stability. On the torus family U$_{\mathrm s}'$ asks that no stable torus-invariant positive-definite idempotent be lighter than $I/c$; the norm form, that none is lighter at all, is a sufficient target, and (4) confines a light one to $y<y_0$.*

#### Lemma L269, a sufficient test for instability of torus-invariant idempotents of so(2m)

*Gate 2 by [r1575](../reviews/REVIEW_r1575.md) and [r1576](../reviews/REVIEW_r1576.md), independently: CONFIRMED, the location of the destabilising direction and the test's sufficiency corrected; both screened $G$ and $L$ from the full product. Derived at the research level in [the D6 working note](../reviews/2026-09-26-I73-class-ceiling.md), batch 46. The algebra is typed in [I117LemmaF.lean](../lean/HamiltonsCubic/Results/I117LemmaF.lean) (`weyl_test`, `det3`, `det3_eq`); the entries of $G$ and $L$ and the sign argument are prose. At the operator level `L269_phi_test` is proved, with the destabilising direction explicit ([r1631](../reviews/REVIEW_r1631.md)); the eigenvalue step and $\Phi=0$ at $I/c$ are prose.*

**Statement.** *Let $C=A\oplus\bigoplus_\alpha\mu_\alpha I_{R_\alpha}$ be a torus-invariant positive-definite idempotent of $\mathfrak{so}(2m)$ ([L268](#lemma-l268-the-torus-invariant-idempotents-of-so2m-are-the-positive-solutions-of-one-cubic-system-in-the-root-weights)), $x=\sum_\alpha\mu_\alpha$, $y=\sum_\alpha\mu_\alpha^2$, $q=\lVert C\rVert^2$, $R=m(m-1)$, $W=4(m-1)y+4(m-2)x$, and*
$$\Phi=(W-R)\Big(\frac{qm}2+2y^2\Big)+x^2(4q-8y-m).$$
*If $\Phi>0$ then $C\#\cdot$ has an eigenvalue above $\tfrac12$ on $C^\perp$, so $C$ is not stable in the sense of [L264](#lemma-l264-statement-h-follows-from-uniqueness-among-stable-positive-definite-idempotents-on-simple-subalgebras); a destabilising direction (a vector of $C^\perp$ with Rayleigh quotient above $\tfrac12$) lies in the projection of $\mathop{\mathrm{span}}\{I_{\mathfrak t},I_R\}$ onto $C^\perp$; the span is not invariant under $C\#\cdot$ unless $C=I/c$. At $I/c$, $\Phi=0$.*

**Proof.** *From L268's formulas, on $\mathop{\mathrm{span}}\{C,I_{\mathfrak t},I_R\}$ the Gram matrix is $G=\begin{pmatrix}q&2y&2x\\2y&m&0\\2x&0&2R\end{pmatrix}$ and $\langle C\#\cdot,\cdot\rangle$ is $L=\begin{pmatrix}q&2y&2x\\2y&0&2x\\2x&2x&W\end{pmatrix}$ ($C\#I_{\mathfrak t}$ has zero Cartan block and root scalars $\mu_\alpha$; $\sum_\alpha u_\alpha u_\alpha^{\mathsf T}=(m-1)I$; each root lies in one pair for each of its $4(m-2)$ neighbours; $\langle C\#C,H\rangle=\langle C,H\rangle$). $C$'s row of $L$ equals its row of $G$, so $\det(L-\lambda G)=(1-\lambda)p(\lambda)$ with $p$ quadratic, leading coefficient $\det G=2mRq-8Ry^2-4mx^2>0$ unless $\mu$ is uniform, and $p(\tfrac12)=-\Phi$. The pencil is $G$-self-adjoint with $C$ an eigenvector of eigenvalue $1$, so its other eigenvectors are $G$-orthogonal to $C$, and $p(\tfrac12)<0$ places one eigenvalue above $\tfrac12$.* $\square$

**What it gives.** *$\Phi>0$ at every non-uniform positive solution found: at least $71$ on $\mathfrak{so}(8)$ with three values of $\Phi$, three types on $\mathfrak{so}(10)$, and [L267](#lemma-l267-u-fails-on-son-itself-for-every-n-at-least-7-unstably-and-heavier-than-in-2)'s for $m=4,\dots,10$. The conjecture $\Phi>0$ at every non-uniform positive solution would give U$_{\mathrm s}$ on the torus-invariant positive-definite idempotents of $\mathfrak{so}(2m)$. It is open because $\Phi$ involves $x$, $y$ and $\lVert A\rVert^2$, which the equations tie together only through higher moments.*

#### Lemma L268, the torus-invariant idempotents of so(2m) are the positive solutions of one cubic system in the root weights

*Gate 2 by [r1572](../reviews/REVIEW_r1572.md) and [r1573](../reviews/REVIEW_r1573.md), independently: CONFIRMED, the scope cut to even $n$ and to idempotency, and a fence-post clause withdrawn; both screened the formulas over all of $\mathop{\mathrm{Sym}}^2$ on $\mathfrak{so}(6)$ and $\mathfrak{so}(8)$. Derived at the research level in [the D6 working note](../reviews/2026-09-26-I73-class-ceiling.md), batch 45. The uniform point is typed in [I117LemmaF.lean](../lean/HamiltonsCubic/Results/I117LemmaF.lean) (`torus_uniform`, `torus_uniform_iff`); in [I117Statements.lean](../lean/HamiltonsCubic/Results/I117Statements.lean) the uniform point solves the system (`L268_uniform`) and positive weights give a positive definite operator (`L268_torus_pd`), both proved, and the uniform clause in both directions in [L268Uniform.lean](../lean/HamiltonsCubic/Results/L268Uniform.lean) (`L268_uniform_iff`: for $m\ge3$, $c>0$, $\mu\equiv1/c$ solves the system exactly when $c=2(m-1)$; `L268_uniform_op` at the operator, [r1736](../reviews/REVIEW_r1736.md)). **L268 is typed in full** in [L268Rep.lean](../lean/HamiltonsCubic/Results/L268Rep.lean): `L268_rep` (every self-adjoint operator commuting with each $\mathrm{ad}_{J_x}$ is `torusOp` of its Gram values, the representation r1629 left owed), `L268_full` (such a $C$ is a positive-definite idempotent exactly when its Cartan block is $A_\mu$ and its weights are positive and solve the system), `Amu_roots` ($A_\mu=2\sum\mu_\alpha^2u_\alpha u_\alpha^{\mathsf T}$) and `display_iff` (the system is the block's per-root display, the identity r1736 left owed). At the operator level `L268_torus_idem` is proved through the adapted-basis build of `𝔰𝔬(2m)` ([r1629](../reviews/REVIEW_r1629.md)), with `L268_pd_pos` for positivity ⇒ $\mu>0$.*

**Statement.** *Fix a maximal torus $T\subset SO(2m)$, $m\ge3$, with Cartan $\mathfrak t=\mathop{\mathrm{span}}(h_i)$ and root planes $R_\alpha$ for the positive roots $\alpha=\varepsilon_i\pm\varepsilon_j$, and let $u_\alpha=\alpha/\sqrt2\in\mathfrak t$. A $T$-invariant symmetric operator is $C=A\oplus\bigoplus_\alpha\mu_\alpha I_{R_\alpha}$, and $C$ is a positive-definite idempotent exactly when $A=2\sum_\alpha\mu_\alpha^2u_\alpha u_\alpha^{\mathsf T}$ and $\mu>0$ solves*
$$\mu_\alpha=4\mu_\alpha^3+\mu_\alpha\sum_{\beta\sim\alpha}\mu_\beta^2+\sum_{\beta\pm\gamma=\pm\alpha}\mu_\beta\mu_\gamma,$$
*$\beta\sim\alpha$ over the $4(m-2)$ positive roots with $\langle\alpha,\beta\rangle=\pm1$, and the last sum over the $2(m-2)$ pairs of positive roots whose sum or difference is $\pm\alpha$. The uniform point $\mu\equiv1/c$ solves it exactly when $c=2(m-1)$.*

**Proof.** *Distinct $T$-weights forbid mixing, and a symmetric operator commuting with a plane rotation is scalar. For an orthonormal basis $p,q$ of $R_\alpha$, $[h_k,p]=\alpha_kq$ and $[h_k,q]=-\alpha_kp$, so $A\#P_\alpha=(u_\alpha^{\mathsf T}Au_\alpha)P_\alpha$; invariance gives $[p,q]=\sum_k\alpha_kh_k=\sqrt2u_\alpha$, so $P_\alpha\#P_\alpha=2u_\alpha u_\alpha^{\mathsf T}$; in $D_m$ a pair $\beta,\gamma$ lands on at most one root plane and never on $\mathfrak t$, the stabiliser of $\alpha$ in the Weyl group is transitive on the $2(m-2)$ pairs, and $I\#I=(2m-2)I$ fixes their common coefficient at $1$. Hence $(C\#C)_{\mathfrak t}=2\sum\mu_\alpha^2u_\alpha u_\alpha^{\mathsf T}$ and $(C\#C)_\alpha=2\mu_\alpha u_\alpha^{\mathsf T}Au_\alpha+\sum\mu_\beta\mu_\gamma$; with $(u_\alpha\cdot u_\beta)^2\in\{1,\tfrac14,0\}$ eliminating $A$ gives the system, and $A\succ0$ when every $\mu_\alpha>0$ since the coroots span $\mathfrak t$.* $\square$

**What it gives.** *The torus-invariant family contains every counterexample to U found; this system holds [L267](#lemma-l267-u-fails-on-son-itself-for-every-n-at-least-7-unstably-and-heavier-than-in-2)'s at even $n$ (screened at $m=4,5,6$), and a screen on $\mathfrak{so}(8)$ finds only $I/c$ stable among its positive solutions. U$_{\mathrm s}$ on torus-invariant idempotents of $\mathfrak{so}(2m)$ is thereby a finite problem on $D_m$, though the stability it asks for is on all of $C^\perp$ and the maximiser need not be torus-invariant.*

#### Lemma L267, U fails on so(n) itself for every n at least 7, unstably and heavier than I/(n-2)

*Gate 2 by [r1569](../reviews/REVIEW_r1569.md) and [r1570](../reviews/REVIEW_r1570.md), independently: CONFIRMED, the symmetry group corrected to $O(n-4)$ for $n=8$, the sign of $b$ and the symmetric branch written in; both screened the idempotent from $\mathfrak{so}(n)$'s bracket. Derived at the research level in [the D6 working note](../reviews/2026-09-26-I73-class-ceiling.md), batch 44. Typed in [I117LemmaF.lean](../lean/HamiltonsCubic/Results/I117LemmaF.lean) (`son_asym_idem`, `son_asym_disc`, `so9_asym_unstable`); the block constants enter as coefficients. At the operator level L267 is proved in [I117Four.lean](../lean/HamiltonsCubic/Results/I117Four.lean): the four-block family is closed under $\#$ with these block equations (`sharp_fourOp`), and `L267_member` states the stated member explicitly and proves it idempotent, positive definite, other than $I/(n-2)$, of the stated norm and norm gap, and unstable along the stated direction projected off $C$ with the stated excess; `L267_U_fails` is its existence form.*

**Statement.** *Under $SU(2)_+\times SU(2)_-\times O(n-4)\subset SO(n)$, $\mathfrak{so}(n)=\Lambda^2_+\oplus\Lambda^2_-\oplus\mathfrak{so}(n-4)\oplus V$, $V=\mathbb R^4\otimes\mathbb R^{n-4}$, and on $C=a_+P_++a_-P_-+a_sP_s+bP_V$ the idempotent equations are $2a_\pm^2+(n-4)b^2=a_\pm$, $(n-6)a_s^2+4b^2=a_s$, $\tfrac32(a_++a_-)+(n-5)a_s=1$. For every $n\ge7$ they have the solution $a_++a_-=\tfrac12$, $a_+a_-=\tfrac{(n-4)b^2}2$, $a_+\ne a_-$, $a_s=\tfrac1{4(n-5)}$, $b=\tfrac{\sqrt{3n-14}}{8(n-5)}$, a positive-definite idempotent on $\mathfrak{so}(n)$ other than $I/(n-2)$. Its squared norm $\tfrac{53n^2-524n+1296}{64(n-5)^2}$ exceeds $\tfrac N{(n-2)^2}$ by $\tfrac{3(n-6)^2(7n^2-44n+48)}{64(n-5)^2(n-2)^2}$, and it is unstable: $C\#\cdot$ has an eigenvalue above $\tfrac12$ on $C^\perp$.*

**Proof.** *The four summands are pairwise non-isomorphic irreducibles, so the family is the invariant one and closed under $\#$. $P_\pm\#P_\pm=2P_\pm$; the trace identity for $\mathfrak{su}(2)_\pm$, $3\cdot4+4(n-4)\kappa=6(n-2)$, gives $\kappa=\tfrac32$ and $P_\pm\#P_V=\tfrac34P_V$; $P_s\#P_s=(n-6)P_s$ and $P_s\#P_V=\tfrac{n-5}2P_V$; the cross products of the three ideals vanish; $I\#I=(n-2)I$ read blockwise gives $P_V\#P_V=(n-4)(P_++P_-)+4P_s$. The $\Lambda^2_\pm$ equations share the quadratic $2a^2-a+(n-4)b^2=0$; taking its two roots fixes $a_s$ and $b^2$ as stated, and the roots are real and distinct when $\tfrac14>2(n-4)b^2$, which is $(n-6)(5n-24)>0$. The norm follows from the weights $3,3,\tfrac{(n-4)(n-5)}2,4(n-4)$. The direction $H=a_-P_++a_+P_-+\tfrac1{n-5}P_s+4bP_V$ has projected excess $\tfrac{9(249n^4-4840n^3+34620n^2-108000n+123840)}{128(n-5)^2(53n^2-524n+1296)}>0$ (the quartic is $249m^4+2132m^3+6186m^2+6828m+1949$ at $m=n-7$), exactly $\tfrac{61389}{198656}$ at $n=9$.* $\square$

**What it gives.** *U fails on the target algebra itself, so [F80](fence-posts.md) binds every argument, including those specific to $\mathfrak{so}(n)$. [L264](#lemma-l264-statement-h-follows-from-uniqueness-among-stable-positive-definite-idempotents-on-simple-subalgebras)'s U$_{\mathrm s}$ is untouched: like [L263](#lemma-l263-u-is-false-by-the-two-component-criterion-at-su3-in-g2)'s idempotents on $\mathfrak g_2$, this one is unstable and heavier than $I/c$. The root starts at the subalgebra idempotent $P_+/2$ of [L259](#lemma-l259-the-quartic-bound-the-internal-cap-and-the-symmetric-family-are-one-norm-statement-for-hamiltons--proved-outside-a-trace-window-of-idempotents), where $\Lambda^2_+$ has Casimir ratio $\tfrac38$ on $V$.*

#### Lemma L266, two constraints on the maximiser: the cubic vanishes on orbit tangents, and a proper support caps the trace by each complementary Casimir

*Gate 2 by [r1565](../reviews/REVIEW_r1565.md) and [r1566](../reviews/REVIEW_r1566.md), independently: both parts CONFIRMED. Derived at the research level in [the D6 working note](../reviews/2026-09-26-I73-class-ceiling.md), batch 42. Part (1) is typed in [I117LemmaF.lean](../lean/HamiltonsCubic/Results/I117LemmaF.lean) as `orbit_tangent_F`, for any derivations of a symmetric associative product, and proved at the operator level on $\mathfrak{so}(n)$ as `L266_orbit_tangent` in [I117Statements.lean](../lean/HamiltonsCubic/Results/I117Statements.lean); part (2) is prose.*

**Statement.** *(1) For every idempotent $C$ on a compact $\mathfrak h$ and $X,Y,Z\in\mathfrak h$, with $u_X=[\mathrm{ad}_X,C]$, $F(u_X,u_Y,u_Z)=0$; so the third-order condition at a maximum of $F$ on the sphere ($F$ vanishes on the $\tfrac12$-eigenspace of $C\#\cdot$) binds only on $\tfrac12$-eigenvectors off the orbit. (2) If the maximiser's idempotent $C$ ([L264](#lemma-l264-statement-h-follows-from-uniqueness-among-stable-positive-definite-idempotents-on-simple-subalgebras) (1)) is supported on a simple $\mathfrak h\subsetneq\mathfrak{so}(n)$, then $\mathop{\mathrm{tr}}C\le d/\mathrm{Cas}_{\mathfrak h}(V)$ for every $\mathfrak h$-invariant irreducible $V\subseteq\mathfrak h^\perp$. At $C=I_{\mathfrak h}/c_{\mathfrak h}$ this reads $\mathrm{Cas}_{\mathfrak h}(V)\le\tfrac12\mathrm{Cas}_{\mathfrak h}(\mathfrak h)$, which a block $\mathfrak{so}(k)\subset\mathfrak{so}(n)$ fails on its vector summands and $G_2\subset SO(7)$ meets with equality.*

**Proof.** *(1) $\mathrm{ad}_X$ acts on $\mathop{\mathrm{Sym}}^2\mathfrak h$ by the derivation $D_X$ of $\#$, so $D_XC=D_X(C\#C)=2C\#D_XC$, and applying $D_Y$, $(1-2L_C)D_YD_XC=2u_X\#u_Y$. Then $F(u_X,u_Y,u_Z)=\tfrac12\langle D_YD_XC,(1-2L_C)u_Z\rangle=0$ by self-adjointness and $L_Cu_Z=\tfrac12u_Z$. Along $C+sH$ with $H$ in the $\tfrac12$-eigenspace, $F(C+sH)/\lVert C+sH\rVert^3=\lVert C\rVert^{-1}\big(1+s^3F(H,H,H)/\lVert C\rVert^2+O(s^4)\big)$, which gives the third-order condition. (2) $P_V\perp C$, and $(P_V\#P_V)_{\mathfrak h\mathfrak h}$ is $\mathfrak h$-equivariant, hence $\beta I_{\mathfrak h}$ with $\beta d=F(P_{\mathfrak h},P_V,P_V)=\tfrac12\mathrm{Cas}_{\mathfrak h}(V)\dim V$; the stability bound $\langle C\#P_V,P_V\rangle=\beta\mathop{\mathrm{tr}}C\le\tfrac12\dim V$ is the claim.* $\square$

**What it gives.** *Part (1) closes the third-order route to U$_{\mathrm s}$ along orbits; part (2) turns any lower bound on the trace of a positive-definite idempotent into a restriction on the maximiser's support, and [L287](#lemma-l287-an-absolute-trace-floor-at-every-positive-semidefinite-idempotent-so-h-asks-nothing-of-an-embedding-of-large-index-the-four-heaviest-exceptional-algebras-among-them)'s floor $\mathop{\mathrm{tr}}C\ge h^\vee/c$ is one: it gives $\mathrm{Cas}_{\mathfrak h}(V)\le dc/h^\vee$ on every such $V$.*

#### Lemma L265, where I/c is stable: I# acts on each component of Sym2 h by c minus a quarter of its Casimir

*Gate 2 by [r1560](../reviews/REVIEW_r1560.md) and [r1561](../reviews/REVIEW_r1561.md), independently: CONFIRMED, one entry corrected ($\mathfrak e_7$'s $\tfrac{14}9$), validated from the inverse Cartan matrix. Derived at the research level in [the D6 working note](../reviews/2026-09-26-I73-class-ceiling.md), batch 41. The arithmetic is typed in [I117LemmaF.lean](../lean/HamiltonsCubic/Results/I117LemmaF.lean) (`so_sharp_scalars`, `stable_iff_cas`, `sp_omega2_unstable`), positivity of $C\#\cdot$ on the PSD cone is proved as `L265_cp` in [I117Statements.lean](../lean/HamiltonsCubic/Results/I117Statements.lean), and on $\mathfrak{so}(n)$ the clauses $I\#I=(n-2)I$, $I\#W=-W$ on Weyl operators and the stability of $I/(n-2)$ as `L265_I_sharp` in [I117Stable.lean](../lean/HamiltonsCubic/Results/I117Stable.lean), through the quadratic form $F(I,H,H)=\tfrac12\big(\sum T_{abcd}T_{acdb}+\lVert\mathrm{Ric}\,T\rVert^2\big)$ of the index array $T_{abcd}=(H\varphi_{cd})_{ab}$ (the form of $I\#H=-H+b(H)+\tfrac12\mathrm{Ric}(H)\wedge g$, $b$ the full cyclic sum over the last three indices) bounded by a square; the formula for general $\mathfrak h$ and the decompositions are quoted representation theory.*

**Statement.** *For $C=\sum_i\lambda_iv_iv_i^{\mathsf T}\succeq0$, $C\#X=\tfrac12\sum_i\lambda_i\mathrm{ad}_{v_i}X\mathrm{ad}_{v_i}^{\mathsf T}$ is completely positive. On a compact simple $\mathfrak h$ ($\mathrm{Cas}_{\mathfrak h}(\mathfrak h)=2c$), $I\#\cdot$ acts on each irreducible $V\subseteq\mathop{\mathrm{Sym}}^2\mathfrak h$ by the scalar $c-\mathrm{Cas}_V/4$, so $I/c$ is stable ($I\#\cdot\le\tfrac c2$ on $I^\perp$) exactly when $\mathrm{Cas}_V\ge\mathrm{Cas}_{\mathfrak h}(\mathfrak h)$ for every nontrivial component. This holds strictly on $\mathfrak{so}(n)$, $n\ge7$, $\mathfrak g_2$, $\mathfrak f_4$, $\mathfrak e_6$, $\mathfrak e_7$, $\mathfrak e_8$ and $\mathfrak{su}(2)$; with equality on the adjoint copy in $\mathop{\mathrm{Sym}}^2\mathfrak{su}(m)$, $m\ge3$; and fails on $\mathfrak{sp}(m)$, $m\ge2$, whose $[\omega_2]$ component has eigenvalue $\tfrac{m+2}{2(m+1)}$.*

**Proof.** *$(vv^{\mathsf T})\#(ww^{\mathsf T})=\tfrac12(\mathrm{ad}_vw)(\mathrm{ad}_vw)^{\mathsf T}$ and both sides of $(vv^{\mathsf T})\#X=\tfrac12\mathrm{ad}_vX\mathrm{ad}_v^{\mathsf T}$ are linear in $X$. Under $\mathop{\mathrm{Sym}}^2\mathfrak h\subset\mathfrak h\otimes\mathfrak h$, $AXA^{\mathsf T}$ is $(A\otimes A)X$, and $\mathrm{Cas}_{\mathfrak h\otimes\mathfrak h}=\mathrm{Cas}\otimes1+1\otimes\mathrm{Cas}-2\sum_k\mathrm{ad}_{e_k}\otimes\mathrm{ad}_{e_k}$, so $I\#\cdot=\tfrac12\sum_k\mathrm{ad}_{e_k}\otimes\mathrm{ad}_{e_k}$ is $\tfrac14(4c-\mathrm{Cas}_V)$ on $V$. On $\mathfrak{so}(n)$ the Casimirs $0$, $2n$, $4n-4$, $4n-16$ give [L259](#lemma-l259-the-quartic-bound-the-internal-cap-and-the-symmetric-family-are-one-norm-statement-for-hamiltons--proved-outside-a-trace-window-of-idempotents)'s $n-2$, $\tfrac{n-4}2$, $-1$, $2$. The ratios $\mathrm{Cas}_V/\mathrm{Cas}_{\mathfrak h}(\mathfrak h)$: $\tfrac{2n}{2n-4}$, $\tfrac{4n-4}{2n-4}$, $\tfrac{4n-16}{2n-4}$ on $\mathfrak{so}(n)$; $1$ on $\mathfrak{su}(m)$'s adjoint copy; $\tfrac m{m+1}$ on $\mathfrak{sp}(m)$'s $[\omega_2]$; $\tfrac76$ and $\tfrac52$ on $\mathfrak g_2$'s $\mathbf{27}$ and $\mathbf{77}$; $2+2/h^\vee$ on the $2\theta$ component and $1+(2h^\vee/3-2)/h^\vee$ ($\tfrac{13}9,\tfrac32,\tfrac{14}9,\tfrac85$) on the other component of $\mathfrak f_4$, $\mathfrak e_6$, $\mathfrak e_7$, $\mathfrak e_8$; $3$ on $\mathfrak{su}(2)$'s spin $2$.* $\square$

**What it gives.** *Where [L264](#lemma-l264-statement-h-follows-from-uniqueness-among-stable-positive-definite-idempotents-on-simple-subalgebras)'s U$_{\mathrm s}$ has content. On $\mathfrak{sp}(m)$, $m\ge2$, $I/c$ is not stable and U$_{\mathrm s}$ implies that no positive-definite idempotent is; on $\mathfrak{sp}(2)=\mathfrak{so}(5)$ the $\Lambda^2_+$ idempotent of L259 already reaches ratio $2/\sqrt3$ against $I/3$'s $3/\sqrt{10}$. On $\mathfrak{su}(m)$ the second-order test at $I/c$ is degenerate, and at $\mathfrak{su}(3)$ a screen ([r1561](../reviews/REVIEW_r1561.md)) finds the cubic nonzero there, so $I/c$ is a third-order saddle. On $\mathfrak g_2$ the eigenvalues $\tfrac5{12}$, $-\tfrac14$ are those found at $I/4$ on L263's and batch 40's invariant spaces.* *The method is Koiso's for the stability of symmetric Einstein metrics, Casimir eigenvalues on the components of $\mathop{\mathrm{Sym}}^2$, transferred to $\#$; the thresholds differ, $SU(n)$ being neutrally stable and $Sp(n)$ stable for scalar curvature ([Lauret–Will](../references/lauret-will-2021-local-maxima-hilbert/notes.md)'s list (3)), against $\mathfrak{su}(m)$ marginal and $\mathfrak{sp}(m)$ unstable here.*

#### Lemma L264, statement H follows from uniqueness among stable positive-definite idempotents on simple subalgebras

*Gate 2 by [r1557](../reviews/REVIEW_r1557.md) and [r1558](../reviews/REVIEW_r1558.md), independently: CONFIRMED, scope corrected to the ansatz in (4), and (2) strengthened by r1557 from $\bigoplus_i\Pi_i/c_i$ to every positive-definite idempotent, validated by the block computation below. Derived at the research level in [the D6 working note](../reviews/2026-09-26-I73-class-ceiling.md), batch 39. Part (4) is typed in [I117LemmaF.lean](../lean/HamiltonsCubic/Results/I117LemmaF.lean) (`sharp_lin`, `twocomp_second_root_peirce`, `g2_su3_peirce`); (1) is proved at the operator level on $\mathfrak{so}(n)$ as `L264_maximiser_stable` and (2) at a split with no cross block as `L264_not_simple_unstable`, both in [I117Statements.lean](../lean/HamiltonsCubic/Results/I117Statements.lean), and (1) in full as `L264_one` in [L264Support.lean](../lean/HamiltonsCubic/Results/L264Support.lean): $C=X/F(X)$ at a positive-semidefinite maximiser is an idempotent, stable and positive semidefinite, with range a perfect subalgebra on which $C$ is positive definite, $F(X)>0$ discharged by `maximiser_pos`. (2) with a cross block is `L264_cross` in [L264Cross.lean](../lean/HamiltonsCubic/Results/L264Cross.lean): for projections onto two orthogonal commuting ideals and a self-adjoint idempotent supported on their sum with both diagonal blocks nonzero, $C$ is unstable for every cross block, through `sharp_blk` ($C\#D_1=D_1$); it needs only both blocks $D_i=P_iCP_i$ nonzero, so it also covers positive-semidefinite idempotents with a cross block, $\Pi_{\mathfrak{so}(3)}$ in $\mathfrak{so}(4)=\Lambda^2_+\oplus\Lambda^2_-$ for one ([r1759](../reviews/REVIEW_r1759.md)). (2) from semisimplicity is `L264_two` in [L264Two.lean](../lean/HamiltonsCubic/Results/L264Two.lean) (for $\mathfrak h=\mathfrak h_1\oplus\mathfrak h_2$, nonzero commuting ideals with $\mathfrak h_1$ perfect, a self-adjoint idempotent with range in $\mathfrak h$, positive definite there, is unstable, the orthogonality, projections and nonzero blocks all derived) and `L264_semisimple_unstable` in [L264Semi.lean](../lean/HamiltonsCubic/Results/L264Semi.lean) (the split from Mathlib's `IsSemisimple` with two distinct atoms), with the destabilising $H=\lVert D_2\rVert^2D_1-\lVert D_1\rVert^2D_2$ exported by `L264_cross_H`, `L264_two_H`, `L264_semisimple_H` in [L264Destab.lean](../lean/HamiltonsCubic/Results/L264Destab.lean) (self-adjoint, orthogonal to $C$, supported on $\mathfrak h$, and $F(C,H,H)>\tfrac12\lVert H\rVert^2$, so $C$ is unstable in $\mathfrak h$'s own sense too, [r1765](../reviews/REVIEW_r1765.md)); and (3)'s sentence whole is `L264_three_full` in [L264Three.lean](../lean/HamiltonsCubic/Results/L264Three.lean) ($C=X/F(X)$ is a stable idempotent, positive definite on its range, which is simple, `L264_three_simple`), with $\mathop{\mathrm{Sym}}^2\mathfrak h$ closed under $\#$ as `sharp_closed` in [L264Sym.lean](../lean/HamiltonsCubic/Results/L264Sym.lean), and the reduction of H to simple ranges as `L270_H_of_Us_simple` in [L270Simple.lean](../lean/HamiltonsCubic/Results/L270Simple.lean). Prose: that $\mathfrak h$'s own $\#$ and stability are read as the ambient ones of $\mathfrak{so}(n)$ (stability ranging over $\mathop{\mathrm{Sym}}^2\mathfrak{so}(n)$ while the destabilising direction lies in $\mathop{\mathrm{Sym}}^2\mathfrak h$), and (3). The step from perfect to semisimple is `L264_one_semisimple` in [CompactSemisimple.lean](../lean/HamiltonsCubic/Results/CompactSemisimple.lean): the range of $C=X/F(X)$ is semisimple.*

**Statement.** *(1) Let $X$ maximise $F$ on the unit sphere of $\mathop{\mathrm{Sym}}^2\mathfrak{so}(n)$, taken positive semidefinite by [L260](#lemma-l260-statement-h-reduces-to-the-least-norm-of-a-positive-definite--idempotent-on-a-semisimple-subalgebra) (1). Then $C=X/F(X)$ is an idempotent, positive definite on the semisimple subalgebra $\mathfrak h$ it is supported on, and* stable: $\langle C\#H,H\rangle\le\tfrac12\lVert H\rVert^2$ *for every $H\perp C$. (2) On a compact semisimple $\mathfrak h$ with two or more simple ideals, no positive-definite idempotent is stable. (3) Hence statement H at $n\ge9$ follows from* **U$_{\mathrm s}$**: *on a compact simple $\mathfrak h$, every stable positive-definite idempotent equals $I/c$. (4) On the ansatz $aP_{\mathfrak k}+bP_{\mathfrak p}$ of a two-component family ([L263](#lemma-l263-u-is-false-by-the-two-component-criterion-at-su3-in-g2)), the second eigenvalue of $C\#\cdot$ on $\mathop{\mathrm{span}}(P_{\mathfrak k},P_{\mathfrak p})$ is $\tfrac12+c(2x-y)(y-x)/(2D)$ at the second root and $(c_1-\alpha)/c$ at $I/c$: for $x<y<2x$ the positive-definite second root is unstable, at $y=2x$ it is $I/c$, and U$_{\mathrm s}$ holds on the ansatz whenever $y\le2x$. Every two-component family whose ambient algebra is $\mathfrak{so}(n)$, $n\ge7$, has $y\le2x$.*

**Proof.** *(1) Lagrange at a maximum gives $X\#X=F(X)X$, and the second variation of $F/\lVert\cdot\rVert^3$ at $C$ along $H\perp C$ is $3\big(\langle C\#H,H\rangle-\tfrac12\lVert H\rVert^2\big)/\lVert C\rVert^3$, which is Theorem A of [Tkachev](../references/tkachev-2024-metrized-slides/notes.md) for the metrized algebra $(\mathop{\mathrm{Sym}}^2\mathfrak{so}(n),\#)$; $\mathop{\mathrm{Sym}}^2\mathfrak h$ is closed under $\#$. (2) Write $C=D_1+D_2+E$ over $\mathfrak h=\mathfrak h_1\oplus\mathfrak h_2$ with $E$ the cross block. $\mathrm{ad}$ kills $\mathfrak h_1\wedge\mathfrak h_2$, so $(C\#C)_{\mathfrak h_i\mathfrak h_i}=D_i\#D_i$ and $C\#D_i=D_i$: each $D_i$ is an eigenvector of eigenvalue $1$, and $\mathop{\mathrm{span}}(D_1,D_2)$ meets $C^\perp$. (3) By (1) and (2) the maximiser's $C$ is a stable positive-definite idempotent on a simple $\mathfrak h\subseteq\mathfrak{so}(n)$, so U$_{\mathrm s}$ gives $C=I/c_{\mathfrak h}$, and [L256](#lemma-l256-the-internal-mass-cap-on-subalgebras-a-closed-e_0oplus-e_2-cannot-reach-theta_0) with $\mathcal M(\mathfrak h)=2c_{\mathfrak h}d$ gives $d/c_{\mathfrak h}^2\ge N/(n-2)^2$. (4) $C\#(uP_{\mathfrak k}+vP_{\mathfrak p})=(ac_1u+bxv)P_{\mathfrak k}+(b\alpha u+(a\alpha+by)v)P_{\mathfrak p}$ has eigenvalue $1$ at $(a,b)$, so its other eigenvalue is the trace minus $1$, on a direction orthogonal to $C$ by self-adjointness; at $y=2x$, $D=xc^2$ and the second root is $(1/c,1/c)$. In $\mathfrak{so}(n)$, $n\ge7$, $I$ is a strict local maximum ([L259](#lemma-l259-the-quartic-bound-the-internal-cap-and-the-symmetric-family-are-one-norm-statement-for-hamiltons--proved-outside-a-trace-window-of-idempotents) (2)), so $(c_1-\alpha)/c\le\tfrac12$, which is $y\le2x$.* $\square$

**What it gives.** *[I117](../plan.md)'s route after U fell: U$_{\mathrm s}$ on simple algebras, and already its weaker form U$_{\mathrm s}'$ ([L270](#lemma-l270-the-direction-i-destabilises-only-idempotents-heavier-than-ic-and-h-asks-only-that-stable-idempotents-are-not-lighter)). It holds on the coordinate-diagonal ([L261](#lemma-l261-u-holds-on-the-coordinate-diagonal-family-the-only-positive-definite-coordinate-diagonal--idempotent-is-in-2)) and single-Casimir symmetric-pair ([L262](#lemma-l262-u-on-symmetric-pair-families-the-only-positive-definite-idempotent-of-the-form-ap_mathfrak-kbp_mathfrak-p-is-ic)) families and on the ansatz of every two-component family with $y\le2x$; both counterexamples to U on $\mathfrak g_2$, L263's and the working note's $SO(4)\subset G_2$ (batch 40), are unstable and heavier than $I/4$. The maximiser is stable on all of $\mathop{\mathrm{Sym}}^2\mathfrak{so}(n)$, more than U$_{\mathrm s}$ uses.*

#### Lemma L263, U is false, by the two-component criterion at SU(3) in G2

*Gate 2 by [r1555](../reviews/REVIEW_r1555.md) and [r1556](../reviews/REVIEW_r1556.md), independently: the criterion CONFIRMED, and both found the counterexample below, which refutes [L260](#lemma-l260-statement-h-reduces-to-the-least-norm-of-a-positive-definite--idempotent-on-a-semisimple-subalgebra)'s U; validated in exact arithmetic from the Casimir ratio and the trace identity, a route sharing nothing with either reviewer's $\mathfrak g_2\subset\mathfrak{so}(7)$ screen. Derived at the research level in [the D6 working note](../reviews/2026-09-26-I73-class-ceiling.md), batch 38. Typed in [I117LemmaF.lean](../lean/HamiltonsCubic/Results/I117LemmaF.lean) (`IsotropyPair`, `pd_unique`, `second_root_idempotent`, `twocomp_second_root_pos`, `g2_su3_witness`) and [I117PsdReduction.lean](../lean/HamiltonsCubic/Results/I117PsdReduction.lean) (`twocomp_factor`, `twocomp_pd_unique`); the block relations at $SU(3)\subset G_2$ are computed from the bracket as `L263_blocks` in [L263Blocks.lean](../lean/HamiltonsCubic/Results/L263Blocks.lean) ($P_{\mathfrak k}\#P_{\mathfrak k}=3P_{\mathfrak k}$, $P_{\mathfrak p}\#P_{\mathfrak p}=P_{\mathfrak k}+\tfrac43P_{\mathfrak p}$, $P_{\mathfrak k}\#P_{\mathfrak p}=\tfrac43P_{\mathfrak p}$, so $c_1=3$, $x=1$, $y=\alpha=\tfrac43$, $c=4$ in $\mathfrak{so}(7)$'s normalisation, by kernel `decide` over the octonion table, [r1774](../reviews/REVIEW_r1774.md)), the Casimir derivation itself being prose. The instance itself is proved from the bracket as `L263_g2` in [I117G2.lean](../lean/HamiltonsCubic/Results/I117G2.lean): an orthogonal basis of $\mathfrak g_2\subset\mathfrak{so}(7)$ from the octonion 3-form, $P_{\mathfrak g_2}\#P_{\mathfrak g_2}=4P_{\mathfrak g_2}$ in $\mathfrak{so}(7)$'s normalisation, and the stated $C$ idempotent, positive definite and heavier. That the witness is not of U's form is proved without identifying $\mathfrak h$ as simple or as $\mathfrak g_2$: `L263_not_ideal_sum` in [L263Ideal.lean](../lean/HamiltonsCubic/Results/L263Ideal.lean) shows $\tfrac9{28}P_{\mathfrak{su}(3)}+\tfrac3{28}P_{\mathfrak p}$ is $\sum\lambda_i\Pi_i$ for no family of projections onto commuting ideals covering $\mathfrak h$, with any weights, since each eigenspace of such a sum is an ideal of $\mathfrak h$ (`eigen_bracket`) and $[g_1,g_3]=3g_4-2g_5$ leaves $\mathfrak p$ (`br13`); the pair $(P_{\mathfrak{su}(3)},P_{\mathfrak p})$ with weights $(\tfrac9{28},\tfrac3{28})$ satisfies $C$'s equation and fails exactly those bracket hypotheses ([r1760](../reviews/REVIEW_r1760.md)). And $\mathfrak h$ has trivial centre, `center_zero`, each coefficient of a central element isolated by one structure constant. $\mathfrak h$ is semisimple in Mathlib's sense, `L263_semisimple` in [CompactSemisimple.lean](../lean/HamiltonsCubic/Results/CompactSemisimple.lean) (from `center_zero` and the definite invariant trace form, through `isSemisimple_of_center`), so the witness is inside U's scope, and `gopC_range` identifies $\mathfrak h$ as the witness's own range ([r1763](../reviews/REVIEW_r1763.md)). What stays with the record: the reading of $\mathfrak h$'s own $\#$ as $\mathfrak{so}(7)$'s restricted to operators supported on $\mathfrak h$ (exact by `sharp_rk1_sum`, in the restricted Frobenius normalisation, $c=4$), the identification with $\mathfrak g_2\supset\mathfrak{su}(3)$, and the Casimir values.*

**Statement.** *Let $\mathfrak g=\mathfrak k\oplus\mathfrak p$ inside a compact simple $\mathfrak g$ ($I\#I=cI$), with $\mathfrak k$ a subalgebra of one induced Casimir value ($P_{\mathfrak k}\#P_{\mathfrak k}=c_1P_{\mathfrak k}$, $c_1>0$) and $\mathfrak p\ne0$ its orthogonal complement, irreducible under $\mathfrak k$. Then $P_{\mathfrak k}\#P_{\mathfrak p}=\alpha P_{\mathfrak p}$ and $P_{\mathfrak p}\#P_{\mathfrak p}=xP_{\mathfrak k}+yP_{\mathfrak p}$, with $c_1=\tfrac12\mathrm{Cas}_{\mathfrak k}(\mathfrak k)$, $\alpha=\tfrac12\mathrm{Cas}_{\mathfrak k}(\mathfrak p)>0$, $c_1+x=c$, $2\alpha+y=c$ and $x\,d_{\mathfrak k}=\alpha\,d_{\mathfrak p}$. The idempotents among $aP_{\mathfrak k}+bP_{\mathfrak p}$ are $0$, $P_{\mathfrak k}/c_1$, $I/c$ and $\big(cx/D,\,c(y-x)/D\big)$, $D=c_1y^2+4\alpha^2x$, the last positive definite exactly when $y>x$, that is $\mathrm{Cas}_{\mathfrak k}(\mathfrak p)<\tfrac12\mathrm{Cas}_{\mathfrak k}(\mathfrak k)$. At $SU(3)\subset G_2$ ($\mathfrak p=\mathbb C^3$), $c=4$, $c_1=3$, $\alpha=\tfrac43$, $x=1$, $y=\tfrac43$, and* $$C=\tfrac9{28}P_{\mathfrak{su}(3)}+\tfrac3{28}P_{\mathfrak p}$$ *is a positive-definite idempotent on $\mathfrak g_2$ other than $I/4$, so U is false. Its squared norm $\tfrac{351}{392}$ exceeds $I/4$'s $\tfrac78$.*

**Proof.** *$P_{\mathfrak k}\#P_{\mathfrak k}$ and $P_{\mathfrak k}\#P_{\mathfrak p}$ are $\tfrac12\mathrm{Cas}_{\mathfrak k}$ on $\mathfrak k$ and on $\mathfrak p$, directly from $(vv^{\mathsf T})\#(ww^{\mathsf T})=\tfrac12[v,w][v,w]^{\mathsf T}$. $\mathrm{Cas}_{\mathfrak k}$ is symmetric and $\mathfrak k$-equivariant, hence scalar on the irreducible $\mathfrak p$ whatever its commutant type, and $I\#I=cI$ read blockwise gives $P_{\mathfrak p}\#P_{\mathfrak p}=(c-c_1)P_{\mathfrak k}+(c-2\alpha)P_{\mathfrak p}$ with no cross block. $\alpha=0$ would make $\mathfrak k$ an ideal. Symmetry of $F$ gives $x\,d_{\mathfrak k}=F(P_{\mathfrak p},P_{\mathfrak p},P_{\mathfrak k})=F(P_{\mathfrak k},P_{\mathfrak p},P_{\mathfrak p})=\alpha\,d_{\mathfrak p}$. On the ansatz $C\#C=C$ is $a^2c_1+b^2x=a$ and $b(2a\alpha+by-1)=0$; for $b\ne0$ these factor as $(cb-1)\big(Db-c(y-x)\big)=0$, and the second root solves both with $a'=cx/D>0$. At $SU(3)\subset G_2$ (the long-root subalgebra, embedding index $1$), in $\mathfrak g_2$'s form with $\mathrm{Cas}_{\mathfrak g}(\mathfrak g)=8$: $\mathrm{Cas}_{\mathfrak k}(\mathfrak k)=6$ and $\mathrm{Cas}_{\mathfrak k}(\mathfrak p)=6\,C_2(\mathbf3)/C_2(\mathbf8)=\tfrac83$, and $8\cdot6+6\cdot\tfrac83=64=2c\,d_{\mathfrak k}$.* $\square$

**What it gives.** *Statement H no longer reduces to U. The counterexample has larger norm than $I/4$, and the second eigenvalue of $C\#\cdot$ on its invariant plane is $\tfrac{15}{28}>\tfrac12$, so it is not a local maximum of $F$ on the sphere; batch 39 of the working note (Gate 2 owed) turns this into the replacement, U$_{\mathrm s}$, on stable idempotents. In ambient $SO(n)$ every computed isotropy-irreducible simple $K$ has ratio at least $\tfrac12$, $G_2\subset SO(7)$ attaining it.* *The symmetric pair $SO(4)\subset G_2$, whose $\mathfrak k=\mathfrak{su}(2)_L\oplus\mathfrak{su}(2)_S$ has two Casimir values, gives a third positive-definite idempotent on $\mathfrak g_2$, $\tfrac{11}{29}P_L+\tfrac5{29}P_S+\sqrt{77/1682}\,P_{\mathfrak p}$, unstable by $\tfrac7{29}$ along an explicit direction and of squared norm $\tfrac{746}{841}>\tfrac78$ ([the working note](../reviews/2026-09-26-I73-class-ceiling.md), batch 40; Gate 2 by [r1560](../reviews/REVIEW_r1560.md) and [r1561](../reviews/REVIEW_r1561.md); `g2_so4_witness`, `g2_so4_unstable`, `g2_so4_norm`), so [L262](#lemma-l262-u-on-symmetric-pair-families-the-only-positive-definite-idempotent-of-the-form-ap_mathfrak-kbp_mathfrak-p-is-ic)'s single-Casimir hypothesis is needed.*

#### Lemma L262, U on symmetric-pair families: the only positive-definite idempotent of the form $aP_{\mathfrak k}+bP_{\mathfrak p}$ is $I/c$

*Gate 2 by [r1226](../reviews/REVIEW_r1226.md) and [r1227](../reviews/REVIEW_r1227.md), independently: CONFIRMED, the hypothesis relaxed and two positivity facts written in. Derived at the research level in [the D6 working note](../reviews/2026-09-26-I73-class-ceiling.md), batch 37. The scalar core is typed as `sympair_idem` in [I117PsdReduction.lean](../lean/HamiltonsCubic/Results/I117PsdReduction.lean), and the case $\mathfrak{so}(n-1)\subset\mathfrak{so}(n)$ is proved at the operator level as `L262_son` in [I117Statements.lean](../lean/HamiltonsCubic/Results/I117Statements.lean).*

**Statement.** *Let $\mathfrak g=\mathfrak k\oplus\mathfrak p$ be a symmetric pair of a compact simple $\mathfrak g$ ($I\#I=cI$, $\mathfrak p\ne0$) whose $\mathfrak k$ has a single induced Casimir value, $P_{\mathfrak k}\#P_{\mathfrak k}=c_{\mathfrak k}P_{\mathfrak k}$, $c_{\mathfrak k}>0$. The idempotents among $aP_{\mathfrak k}+bP_{\mathfrak p}$ are $0$, $P_{\mathfrak k}/c_{\mathfrak k}$, $I/c$ and $\sigma/c$, so [L260](#lemma-l260-statement-h-reduces-to-the-least-norm-of-a-positive-definite--idempotent-on-a-semisimple-subalgebra)'s U holds on this ansatz.*

**Proof.** *The bracket relations and $I\#I=cI$ give $P_{\mathfrak k}\#P_{\mathfrak p}=\tfrac c2P_{\mathfrak p}$ and $P_{\mathfrak p}\#P_{\mathfrak p}=(c-c_{\mathfrak k})P_{\mathfrak k}$, with $c-c_{\mathfrak k}>0$ because $[\mathfrak p,\mathfrak p]\oplus\mathfrak p$ is an ideal, hence $[\mathfrak p,\mathfrak p]=\mathfrak k$. Then $C\#C=C$ reads $a^2c_{\mathfrak k}+b^2(c-c_{\mathfrak k})=a$ and $abc=b$, whose solutions are the four listed.* $\square$

**What it gives.** *With [L261](#lemma-l261-u-holds-on-the-coordinate-diagonal-family-the-only-positive-definite-coordinate-diagonal--idempotent-is-in-2), U on two further families, every idempotent in each being subalgebra-supported, the identity's multiple, or its automorphism twist. The ansatz is not argued to exhaust the $K$-invariant operators.*

#### Lemma L261, U holds on the coordinate-diagonal family: the only positive-definite coordinate-diagonal $\#$-idempotent is $I/(n-2)$

*Gate 2 by [r1224](../reviews/REVIEW_r1224.md) and [r1225](../reviews/REVIEW_r1225.md), independently: CONFIRMED, one unstated link written in. Derived at the research level in [the D6 working note](../reviews/2026-09-26-I73-class-ceiling.md), batch 34. The step after Perron–Frobenius is typed as `delta_scalar` in [I117PsdReduction.lean](../lean/HamiltonsCubic/Results/I117PsdReduction.lean), and the whole lemma is proved at the operator level as `L261_coord_diag` in [I117Statements.lean](../lean/HamiltonsCubic/Results/I117Statements.lean), with the Perron vector from `perron_positive_eigvec`.*

**Statement.** *If $C=\sum_{i<k}a_{ik}\,e_{ik}e_{ik}^{\mathsf T}$ on $\mathfrak{so}(n)$ has every $a_{ik}>0$ and $C\#C=C$, then $C=I/(n-2)$.*

**Proof.** *With $W$ the symmetric zero-diagonal matrix of the $a_{ik}$, $C\#C$ is diagonal with entries $(W^2)_{ik}$, so $C\#C=C$ is $W^2=W+\Delta$, $\Delta=\mathop{\mathrm{diag}}(\sum_ja_{ij}^2)$. $W$ is irreducible, so Perron–Frobenius gives a simple eigenvalue $\rho$ with eigenvector $v>0$, and $W^2v=Wv+\Delta v$ makes $\Delta=(\rho^2-\rho)I$ (`delta_scalar`). Since $\Delta>0$, $\rho>1$, so $W$ has the distinct eigenvalues $\rho$ (simple) and $1-\rho$, $W=(1-\rho)I+(2\rho-1)vv^{\mathsf T}$. The zero diagonal forces $v=\mathbf1/\sqrt n$, and $W=(J-I)/(n-2)$.* $\square$

**What it gives.** *U, hence [L260](#lemma-l260-statement-h-reduces-to-the-least-norm-of-a-positive-definite--idempotent-on-a-semisimple-subalgebra)'s statement H, on the whole coordinate-diagonal family. Positive definiteness is load-bearing: $\Pi_{\mathfrak{so}(m)}/(m-2)$ is a PSD coordinate-diagonal idempotent. Off the family, the missing input is an independent description of $E-\tfrac12Z$ (batch 34), the Perron eigen-equation itself being automatic.*

#### Lemma L260, statement H reduces to the least norm of a positive-definite $\#$-idempotent on a semisimple subalgebra

*Gate 2 by [r1222](../reviews/REVIEW_r1222.md) and [r1223](../reviews/REVIEW_r1223.md), independently: the reduction CONFIRMED, the trace equivalence repaired from simple to semisimple algebras, the stated obstacle restated; U, stated here as sufficient, refuted by [r1555](../reviews/REVIEW_r1555.md) and [r1556](../reviews/REVIEW_r1556.md) ([L263](#lemma-l263-u-is-false-by-the-two-component-criterion-at-su3-in-g2)). Derived at the research level in [the D6 working note](../reviews/2026-09-26-I73-class-ceiling.md), batch 33. The scalar steps are typed in [I117PsdReduction.lean](../lean/HamiltonsCubic/Results/I117PsdReduction.lean) (`weighted_amgm`, `three_ratio`, `trace_le_of_pairing`); (1) at self-adjoint $X$ is proved as `L260_psd_cone` (in $X$'s eigenframe) and (3)'s reduction as `L260_H_of_norm_bound`, both in [I117Statements.lean](../lean/HamiltonsCubic/Results/I117Statements.lean); (2) as `L260_range_subalgebra` and (4) as `L260_trace_bound` are proved in the same file, so every typed clause is machine-checked at $\mathfrak h=\mathfrak{so}(n)$ and self-adjoint $X$ ((3) in the direction H needs); (4)'s converse is proved in [L260Converse.lean](../lean/HamiltonsCubic/Results/L260Converse.lean) (`idem_Ic`, `pd_Ic`: $I/(n-2)$ is a self-adjoint positive-definite idempotent; `L260_trace_eq_iff`: among those, $(n-2)\mathop{\mathrm{tr}}C=N$ exactly at $C=I/(n-2)$, [r1738](../reviews/REVIEW_r1738.md)), so (4) at $\mathfrak{so}(n)$ is an equivalence. (1) at every $X$, in the rows-together convention the statement names, is `L260_one` in [L260One.lean](../lean/HamiltonsCubic/Results/L260One.lean): the positive-semidefinite square roots $\lvert X\rvert$, $\lvert X^{\mathsf T}\rvert$ exist and $F(X)=$ `Ftri X Xᵀ X` $\le\tfrac12(F(\lvert X^{\mathsf T}\rvert)+F(\lvert X\rvert))$, through an SVD built from $X^{\mathsf T}X$'s eigenbasis and termwise AM-GM. (2)'s semisimple clause is `L260_two_semisimple` (the range of every self-adjoint positive-semidefinite idempotent is semisimple in Mathlib's sense), and (4)'s inequality at a proper $\mathfrak h$ is `L260_four_sub` in [L260Sub.lean](../lean/HamiltonsCubic/Results/L260Sub.lean): in an eigenbasis of any positive-semidefinite $\#$-idempotent $C$, $\mathop{\mathrm{tr}}(CK)\le d$ with $K$ the Casimir diagonal of $\mathfrak h=\mathop{\mathrm{range}}C$, $K=P_{\mathfrak h}\#P_{\mathfrak h}=\bigoplus_ic_i\Pi_i$ (`Kh_eq_sharp`, [r1764](../reviews/REVIEW_r1764.md)), and `L260_four` states it for every self-adjoint positive-semidefinite idempotent with the eigenbasis supplied, the pairing (`pairing_gen`) needing no positivity off the range. (4)'s equality case at a proper $\mathfrak h$ is prose.*

**Statement.** *(1) For every $X\in\mathop{\mathrm{End}}(\mathfrak{so}(n))$, with $\#$ extended off $\mathop{\mathrm{Sym}}^2$ by $(X\#Y)_{\alpha\beta}=\tfrac12c_\alpha^{\gamma\delta}c_\beta^{\varepsilon\zeta}X_{\gamma\varepsilon}Y_{\delta\zeta}$ (the rows of both factors in one structure constant, so $F(X)=\tfrac12\sum\sigma_a\sigma_b\sigma_c\langle u_c,[u_a,u_b]\rangle\langle v_c,[v_a,v_b]\rangle$ at $X=\sum\sigma_au_a\otimes v_a$), $F(X)\le\tfrac12\big(F(|X^{\mathsf T}|)+F(|X|)\big)$, so [L259](#lemma-l259-the-quartic-bound-the-internal-cap-and-the-symmetric-family-are-one-norm-statement-for-hamiltons--proved-outside-a-trace-window-of-idempotents)'s $\kappa_n$ is attained on the PSD cone and a norm-minimal $\#$-idempotent can be taken PSD. (2) The range of a PSD idempotent is a semisimple subalgebra $\mathfrak h$, on which it is a positive-definite idempotent of $\mathfrak h$'s own $\#$. (3) Hence $1/\kappa_n^2$ is the least squared norm of a positive-definite idempotent on a compact semisimple subalgebra of $\mathfrak{so}(n)$, so statement H for $n\ge9$ is the bound $\lVert C\rVert^2\ge N/(n-2)^2$ on those idempotents, which every $\bigoplus_i\Pi_i/c_i$ ($\Pi_i\#\Pi_i=c_i\Pi_i$) meets by [L256](#lemma-l256-the-internal-mass-cap-on-subalgebras-a-closed-e_0oplus-e_2-cannot-reach-theta_0). (4) Every positive-definite idempotent satisfies $\mathop{\mathrm{tr}}(CK)\le d$, $K=\bigoplus_ic_i\Pi_i$, with equality exactly at $\bigoplus_i\Pi_i/c_i$.*

> **The convention in (1) is load-bearing off $\mathop{\mathrm{Sym}}^2$.** *In the layer's operator convention, $\langle(A\#B)\varphi,\psi\rangle=\tfrac12\sum\langle[A\omega_a,B\omega_b],\varphi\rangle\langle[\omega_a,\omega_b],\psi\rangle$, the matrix of $X\#X$ is the transpose of the one above, $F$ pairs rows with columns, and (1) fails: a screen of 2000 random $X$ on $\mathfrak{so}(4)$ gives a gap up to $+24.5$ there against at most $-6.3$ in the convention above, the two agreeing on $\mathop{\mathrm{Sym}}^2$ (the lean session's finding, reproduced independently). In the convention above $F(X)=F(X^{\mathsf T})$, the SVD formula being symmetric in $u$ and $v$, so which index counts as the row does not change the value ([r1762](../reviews/REVIEW_r1762.md)). Nothing downstream moves: $\kappa_n$ is a supremum over $\mathop{\mathrm{Sym}}^2$, and (1) is used only at self-adjoint $X$, where `L260_psd_cone` proves it.*

**Proof.** (1) Expand $F$ over the singular value decomposition and apply $ab\le\tfrac12(a^2+b^2)$ to each term, whose weight $\sigma_k\sigma_l\sigma_m$ is nonnegative. A PSD maximiser is then a global maximiser, hence critical. (2) $C\#C=\tfrac12\sum\sigma_i\sigma_j|[v_i,v_j]\rangle\langle[v_i,v_j]|$ has range $[V,V]$, which must equal $V$, and a perfect subalgebra of a compact algebra is semisimple. (3) Both directions of the minimum hold, and $\bigoplus_i\Pi_i/c_i$ has squared norm $\sum_id_i/c_i^2\ge N/(n-2)^2$ by [L256](#lemma-l256-the-internal-mass-cap-on-subalgebras-a-closed-e_0oplus-e_2-cannot-reach-theta_0). (4) Pair $C\#C=C$ with $C^{-1}$ in $C$'s eigenframe, symmetrise each triple, use $\frac{xy}z+\frac{yz}x+\frac{zx}y\ge x+y+z$ and the row sums $\sum_{i,j}\langle[u_i,u_j],u_k\rangle^2=2\langle Ku_k,u_k\rangle$. Equality forces the eigenvalues constant on bracket-connected triples, which on each simple ideal connect everything. $\square$

**What it gives.** *Statement H, and with it [I117](../plan.md)'s sharp $\#$ half of P and [I114](../plan.md), is a lower bound on the norm of positive-definite idempotents on semisimple subalgebras. The uniqueness that would have given it, U (the positive-definite idempotents on $\mathfrak h$ are exactly $\bigoplus_i\Pi_i/c_i$), is false: [L263](#lemma-l263-u-is-false-by-the-two-component-criterion-at-su3-in-g2)'s $SU(3)\subset G_2$ gives a second one on $\mathfrak g_2$, of larger norm than $I/4$. Pairings of $C\#C=C$ bound the trace from above and the norm from below relative to the trace ($\mathop{\mathrm{tr}}C\le c\lVert C\rVert^2$). The diagonal at the largest eigenvalue bounds the trace from below, $\mathop{\mathrm{tr}}C\ge h^\vee/c$ ([L287](#lemma-l287-an-absolute-trace-floor-at-every-positive-semidefinite-idempotent-so-h-asks-nothing-of-an-embedding-of-large-index-the-four-heaviest-exceptional-algebras-among-them)).*

#### Lemma L259, the quartic bound, the internal cap and the symmetric family are one norm statement for Hamilton's $\#$, proved outside a trace window of idempotents

*Gate 2 by [r1220](../reviews/REVIEW_r1220.md) and [r1221](../reviews/REVIEW_r1221.md), independently: C1, C2, C5, C6 CONFIRMED, the failure range narrowed to $4\le n\le8$, the lower window end qualified to even $n$, the screen PLAUSIBLE. Derived at effort `xhigh` in [the D6 working note](../reviews/2026-09-26-I73-class-ceiling.md), batch 30. The scalar core is typed in [I73SharpNorm.lean](../lean/HamiltonsCubic/Results/I73SharpNorm.lean) (`idem_norm_eq`, `outer_bound`, `window_bound`, `idem_norm_ge`); the pairing identity enters as a hypothesis.*

**Statement.** *On $\mathop{\mathrm{Sym}}^2\mathfrak{so}(n)$ with the Frobenius norm, let $\kappa_n=\sup F(X,X,X)/\lVert X\rVert^3$, $F(A,B,C)=\langle A\#B,C\rangle$. (1) $\kappa_n=\sup\lVert X\#Y\rVert/(\lVert X\rVert\lVert Y\rVert)$, and [L257](#lemma-l257-the-internal-mass-cap-follows-from-a-quartic-bound-on-pipi)'s quartic bound, [L255](#lemma-l255-the-symmetric-three-value-family-reduces-to-an-internal-bracket-mass-cap-and-thetale4)'s internal cap and I73's symmetric family ($\theta=F(W,W,W)$ there) each follow from* **statement H**, $\kappa_n=(n-2)/\sqrt N$ for $n\ge9$, *which is false for $4\le n\le8$ and true at $n=3$. (2) Every symmetric orthogonal automorphism $\sigma$ has $\sigma\#\sigma=(n-2)\sigma$, so $I$ and every involutive $\mathrm{Ad}_g$ attain $(n-2)/\sqrt N$; $I$ is a strict local maximum for $n\ge7$. (3) Every $\#$-idempotent $C$ with trace $s$ has $\lVert C\rVert^2=\tfrac{2s^2}n-s+\tfrac{n-2}2\lVert C_{\mathrm{Ric}_0}\rVert^2+3\lVert C_{\Lambda^4}\rVert^2$, and for $n\ge9$ it satisfies H ($\lVert C\rVert^2\ge N/(n-2)^2$) unless $-\tfrac n{2(n-2)}<s<\tfrac{(n-1)(n-4)}{2(n-2)}$.*

**Proof.** (1) Banach's theorem for symmetric trilinear forms on a real Hilbert space; the three reductions are $\lVert\Pi\#\Pi\rVert\le\kappa_nd$, $F(\Pi,\Pi,\Pi)=\mathcal M(V)/2$, and $\mathop{\mathrm{tr}}W^3=0$ at $W=a(P_2-P_0)$ with $\mathop{\mathrm{tr}}W=0$ forcing equal multiplicities; with [L256](#lemma-l256-the-internal-mass-cap-on-subalgebras-a-closed-e_0oplus-e_2-cannot-reach-theta_0)'s `whole_below_theta0`, H gives $\theta<\theta_0$. At $n=3$, $X^\#=\mathop{\mathrm{cof}}X$ and $F=3\det X$. (2) $\mathrm{ad}\circ(\sigma\wedge\sigma)=\sigma\circ\mathrm{ad}$ and $\mathrm{ad}\,\mathrm{ad}^{\mathsf T}=(n-2)I$; the second variation at $I$ needs each traceless scalar of $I\#$ ($\tfrac{n-4}2,-1,2$) strictly below $\tfrac{n-2}2$. (3) Pair $C\#C=C$ with $I$, use $I\#$'s scalars $n-2,\tfrac{n-4}2,-1,2$, eliminate the Weyl norm, and minimise over the free components with the Weyl norm nonnegative (`idem_norm_ge`). $\square$

**What it gives.** *One statement, H, in place of three, with a proved equality set strictly larger than $\{I\}$ (every involutive automorphism, both ends of the window at even $n$), and a proof of H on every idempotent outside the window. The window is where the automorphism family attains equality, and the next invariant pairings need $F$ on the Weyl component, the $\#$ half of Problem P's cubic. Screens at $n=9,10,12$ find no violator ([screen_I73_sharp_cubic_norm.py](../scripts/misc/screen_I73_sharp_cubic_norm.py), [r1220](../reviews/REVIEW_r1220.md)'s 72 extra ascents). I73's symmetric family is blocked on H as [I114](../plan.md).*

#### Lemma L245, the isotropy of each eigenspace is capped direction by direction, and a star eigenspace must be small

*Gate 2 by [r1132](../reviews/REVIEW_r1132.md) and [r1133](../reviews/REVIEW_r1133.md), independently: every item CONFIRMED except the survivor count, PLAUSIBLE and repaired below. The scalar core is typed in [L245SymmetricIsotropy.lean](../lean/HamiltonsCubic/Results/L245SymmetricIsotropy.lean), and the lemma at the operator level in [L245Op.lean](../lean/HamiltonsCubic/Results/L245Op.lean): the two identities as `L245_op`, the three ceilings as `L245_ceilings`, and the star clause in every class as `L245_star`, `L245_star_two` and `L245_star_one` ([r1781](../reviews/REVIEW_r1781.md), [r1782](../reviews/REVIEW_r1782.md), [r1783](../reviews/REVIEW_r1783.md)). The hypothesis it takes is the existence of the eigenprojectors of a three-valued $W$, which is the spectral theorem.*

**Statement.** Let $W$ be a unit critical point with three eigenvalues $\lambda_0<\lambda_1<\lambda_2$ and isotropy matrices $A_c=\sum_{\psi\in E_c}\psi\psi^{\mathsf T}$ on $\mathbb R^n$ (the record's $A_a$, [the summed-pair note](../reviews/2026-09-23-I73c-summed-pairs.md), Setting). For every unit $x\in\mathbb R^n$ the weights $a_c=\langle A_cx,x\rangle$ satisfy
$$a_0+a_1+a_2=n-1,\qquad\lambda_0a_0+\lambda_1a_1+\lambda_2a_2=0,\qquad a_c\ge0,$$
so each $a_c$ is at most $h_c$, the largest value of that coordinate on the resulting segment: $h_0=\frac{(n-1)\lambda_2}{\lambda_2-\lambda_0}$, $h_2=\frac{(n-1)\lvert\lambda_0\rvert}{\lambda_2-\lambda_0}$, and $h_1=\frac{(n-1)\lvert\lambda_0\rvert}{\lambda_1-\lambda_0}$ if $\lambda_1\ge0$, $\frac{(n-1)\lambda_2}{\lambda_2-\lambda_1}$ otherwise. *Only $\mathop{\mathrm{Ric}}(W)=0$ is used, so the lemma holds at every three-valued Weyl tensor, critical or not.* **In particular, if $E_c\supseteq e\wedge U$ for a unit $e$ and a subspace $U\perp e$, then $\dim U\le h_c$.** At a symmetric spectrum $(-a,0,a)$, $A_0=A_2$ and $h_0=h_2=\tfrac{n-1}2$.

**Proof.** $\sum_cA_c=\mathop{\mathrm{Ric}}(\mathrm{Id})=(n-1)I$ and $\sum_c\lambda_cA_c=\mathop{\mathrm{Ric}}(W)=0$ (section 3 of the note), and each $A_c\succeq0$; evaluate at $x$. For the star, $(e\wedge u)(e\wedge u)^{\mathsf T}e=e$ for unit $u\perp e$, so $\langle A_ce,e\rangle\ge\dim U$. $\square$

**What it does.** *The per-vector caps of [L244](#lemma-l244-the-per-vector-ceiling-on-a-symmetric-three-value-critical-point) pass eigenspaces built from decomposable elements, and the two explicit all-good subspaces the approved screen met, $e_1\wedge U$ with $\dim U=8$ at $(8,50,8)$ and $e_1\wedge\mathbb R^{11}$ plus one direction at $(12,42,12)$, are stars with $\dim U>\tfrac{11}2$, so this lemma excludes both.* **Sharp at $S^5\times S^7$**: its $A_c$ are $4P_5$, $6P_7$ and $7P_5+5P_7$, so $h=(7,6,4)$ is the top eigenvalue of each, attained by stars in all three classes ([r1132](../reviews/REVIEW_r1132.md), [r1133](../reviews/REVIEW_r1133.md)). **Across the 190 survivors at $n=12$, $\theta=1.2361$, the smaller extreme eigenspace cannot be a star at 175, of which 63 only because $m_c>n-1$ and 112 by this lemma ($h_c<m_c\le n-1$)** ([screen_I73c_star_isotropy.py](../scripts/misc/screen_I73c_star_isotropy.py), a screen). *"Cannot be a star" means that $E_c$ is not itself a star; it excludes subspaces, not spectra.* *It is the first ingredient that constrains the eigenspaces JOINTLY, through $\mathop{\mathrm{Ric}}(W)=0$, which the per-vector caps do not see.* **Owed**: the combination, an all-good $E_c$ with $A_c\preceq h_cI$ as an operator inequality, beyond stars.


#### Lemma L246, the isotropy of two eigenspaces together is capped by the directions where it is small

*Gate 2 by [r1134](../reviews/REVIEW_r1134.md) and [r1135](../reviews/REVIEW_r1135.md), independently: the inequality and the corollary CONFIRMED; the Hodge-star sharpness point and the scope sentence refuted and repaired below. The scalar steps are typed in [L246PairIsotropy.lean](../lean/HamiltonsCubic/Results/L246PairIsotropy.lean); the isotropy matrices and the Loewner steps enter as hypotheses.*

**Statement.** Let $W$ be a Weyl tensor with three eigenvalues $\lambda_0<\lambda_1<\lambda_2$ and isotropy matrices $A_c$ as in [L245](#lemma-l245-the-isotropy-of-each-eigenspace-is-capped-direction-by-direction-and-a-star-eigenspace-must-be-small). The $A_c$ commute, and for any two classes $a\ne b$, with $\beta_1,\dots,\beta_n$ the eigenvalues of $A_a+A_b$ on a common eigenbasis,
$$\beta_j\le\sum_{i\ne j}\min(1,\beta_i)\qquad\text{for every }j.$$
The inequality holds for $A_F=\sum_{\psi}\psi^{\mathsf T}\psi$ over an orthonormal basis of any subspace $F\subseteq\Lambda^2$ and any eigenvector of $A_F$, and uses $\mathop{\mathrm{Ric}}(W)=0$ only through the common eigenbasis. The corollary is where $\mathop{\mathrm{Ric}}(W)=0$ does the work.
**Corollary.** If an extreme eigenspace is a star, $E_0=e\wedge U$ with $U\subseteq e^\perp$, $1\le\dim U=p\le n-1$, then $\lambda_1<0$ (and $\lambda_1>0$ for a star $E_2$). **So on the symmetric family, and whenever $\lambda_1=0$, no extreme eigenspace is a star, at every $n$.** *At $\lambda_1=0$ the inequality on the eigenvalues $\alpha$ of $A_0$ reads $\alpha_j\le\sum_{i\ne j}\min(\tfrac1{1+r},\alpha_i)$, $r=\lvert\lambda_0\rvert/\lambda_2$.*

**Remark, the support count** ([r1136](../reviews/REVIEW_r1136.md), [r1137](../reviews/REVIEW_r1137.md): true, sharp, and a dimension count rather than a consequence of the inequality). At $\lambda_1=0$, with $r=\lvert\lambda_0\rvert/\lambda_2$, $A_2=rA_0$ has the range of $A_0$, so $E_0\subseteq\Lambda^2S$ forces $E_0\oplus E_2\subseteq\Lambda^2S$ with $\dim E_2=rp$: **$(1+r)p\le\tfrac{s(s-1)}2$, so $E_0$ fills at most a $\tfrac1{1+r}$ share and $E_2$ at most a $\tfrac r{1+r}$ share of any $\Lambda^2S$ containing it**, and $E_0=\Lambda^2\mathbb R^s$ is impossible. Equality holds exactly when $\Lambda^2S=E_0\oplus E_2$, e.g. $W^\pm=\mathop{\mathrm{diag}}(2,-1,-1)$ on $\mathbb R^4$, padded; such points are padded two-value critical points when critical, bounded by [L105](bounds-relaxations.md).

**Proof.** $\sum_cA_c=(n-1)I$ and $\sum_c\lambda_cA_c=0$ make each $A_c$ affine in $A_0$, so they commute. Fix a common unit eigenvector $e$ and put $M_c(e)=\sum_{\psi\in E_c}(\psi e)(\psi e)^{\mathsf T}$ on $e^\perp$. Summing over an orthonormal basis of $\Lambda^2$ gives $\sum_cM_c(e)=I-ee^{\mathsf T}$, so $X=M_a(e)+M_b(e)\preceq I$. For $z\perp e$, skew-symmetry and Cauchy–Schwarz give $\langle\psi e,z\rangle^2=\langle e,\psi z\rangle^2\le\lvert\psi z\rvert^2$, so $M_c(e)\preceq\hat A_c$, the compression to $e^\perp$, and $X\preceq\hat A_a+\hat A_b$, whose eigenvalues are the $\beta_i$, $i\ne j$. Let $Q$ project onto those with $\beta_i\ge1$: $\mathop{\mathrm{tr}}X=\mathop{\mathrm{tr}}QXQ+\mathop{\mathrm{tr}}(I-Q)X(I-Q)\le\mathop{\mathrm{tr}}Q+\sum_{\beta_i<1}\beta_i$, and $\mathop{\mathrm{tr}}X=\langle(A_a+A_b)e,e\rangle=\beta_j$. *Corollary*: on $Z=(e\oplus U)^\perp\ne0$, $A_0=0$, so $(\lambda_2-\lambda_1)A_2=-\lambda_1(n-1)I$ there, and $A_2\succeq0$ forces $\lambda_1\le0$ when $p\le n-2$ ($Z\ne0$); at $p=n-1$, $\langle A_1e,e\rangle=-(n-1)\lvert\lambda_0\rvert/(\lambda_2-\lambda_1)<0$ excludes the star outright. At $\lambda_1=0$, $A_2=0$ on $Z$, the pair $(0,2)$ has $\beta=0$ on $Z$ and $\min(1,\beta)\le1$ on $U$, while at $e$, $\beta=p+\tfrac{\lvert\lambda_0\rvert}{\lambda_2}p>p$, which contradicts the inequality. $\square$

**Sharp** at $S^5\times S^7$: with $A_c$ equal to $4P_5$, $6P_7$ and $7P_5+5P_7$ ([L245](#lemma-l245-the-isotropy-of-each-eigenspace-is-capped-direction-by-direction-and-a-star-eigenspace-must-be-small)), the pair of classes of multiplicities $10$ and $35$ has $\beta=11$ on $\mathbb R^5$, $5$ on $\mathbb R^7$, and $11=4+7$. The pair $21,35$ is equal on $\mathbb R^7$ in the same way. *A pair making up all of $\Lambda^2$ has $A_a+A_b=(n-1)I$ and equality automatically, so only pairs within three classes test sharpness.* *Gate 1: (a) and (c) by the two $S^5\times S^7$ pairs, (d) against L245's ceiling, which the single-class form $\alpha_j\le\sum_{i\ne j}\min(1,\alpha_i)$ does not exceed.*

**What it does.** *Applied to $F=E_0\oplus E_2$ with $\mathop{\mathrm{Ric}}(W)=0$, which at $\lambda_1=0$ gives $A_2=\tfrac{\lvert\lambda_0\rvert}{\lambda_2}A_0$, it is the first ingredient on file that couples two eigenspaces, at every $n$.* **It removes every configuration in [F77](fence-posts.md#post-f77-r39s-per-eigenspace-ingredients-leave-a-window-of-symmetric-spectra-open-at-every-n-from-19)'s window**, the stars there being symmetric. **Owed**: its reach beyond stars, where the per-vector caps admit near-decomposable eigenspaces. *Heuristically*, a near-star at $\lambda_1=0$ has $\beta_e\approx(1+r)p$ against a capped remainder near $p$, a margin of $rp$ rather than L245's $p-h_c$. *Exactly*, $\alpha_{\max}\le\frac{N}{1+r}+S$, with $N$ the number of other eigenvalues of $A_0$ at least $\tfrac1{1+r}$ and $S$ the sum of the rest.



#### Lemma L247, on the symmetric family the pair operator's bracket part is forced above maximality's bound where $\theta\sqrt{2p}>n-5$

*Derived 2026-09-25 in [the per-vector note](../reviews/2026-09-25-I73c-per-vector-rows.md). **Gate 2**: the rank certificate by [r1146](../reviews/REVIEW_r1146.md), [r1147](../reviews/REVIEW_r1147.md); the class-trace form and the identity by [r1149](../reviews/REVIEW_r1149.md), [r1150](../reviews/REVIEW_r1150.md); the band and its margin by [r1155](../reviews/REVIEW_r1155.md), [r1156](../reviews/REVIEW_r1156.md). Typed in [ClassTraceTest.lean](../lean/HamiltonsCubic/Results/ClassTraceTest.lean) on the traced rows as hypotheses.*

**Statement.** On the symmetric three-value family ($\lambda=(-a,0,a)$ on $E_0,E_1,E_2$, $\dim E_0=\dim E_2=p$, $a=1/\sqrt{2p}$, $\kappa=\theta\sqrt{2p}$), with $t_{abc}=\mathop{\mathrm{tr}}(P_aB_{bc})$ and $D=\tfrac12(t_{222}-t_{002}+p)$, the nine traced rows give
$$16D=(n-5-\kappa)\,p-4t_{000}-t_{011}-4t_{122}.$$
The leading (bracket) part $B_{\mathrm{lead}}$ of the pair matrix on $\pi_{\mathcal W}(E_0\odot E_2)$ has $\lambda_{\max}(B_{\mathrm{lead}})\ge T/p$, and $2T/p-\tfrac12=-D/(p\kappa)$. **So wherever $\theta\sqrt{2p}>n-5$, $\lambda_{\max}(L_e)$'s bracket part exceeds maximality's bound $\tfrac12$ by at least $(\kappa-(n-5))/(16\kappa)$.**

**Proof.** The rows force $\mathop{\mathrm{rank}}P_0C=p$ by ad-invariance and L244's floor $b_{20}>0$, and $B_{\mathrm{lead}}\preceq M_0$ of that rank, so its positive eigenvalues number at most $p$ and sum to at least $T$. Row (C$_0$) traced gives $4T=p+\kappa^{-1}(t_{000}-t_{022}+p)$; the nine traced rows have rank $7$ and give the identity; nonnegativity of $t_{000},t_{011},t_{122}$ gives the bound. $\square$

**Extension to general three-value spectra** ([r1170](../reviews/REVIEW_r1170.md), [r1171](../reviews/REVIEW_r1171.md)). For $\lambda_0<\lambda_1<\lambda_2$ with $\lambda_0<0<\lambda_2$, $\theta>0$ and $\lambda_1\ge0$, the bracket part's positive part comes from $E_0$ alone, so $\lambda_{\max}\ge T/p_0$ with $T=-\tfrac1{2\theta}\sum_c\lambda_ct_{02c}$ (since $\mathop{\mathrm{rank}}P_0C\le p_0$). The six traced rows of $E_0$ and $E_1$ give $\theta(\lambda_0-\lambda_1)(4T-p_0)=\mathrm{const}+c_{000}t_{000}+c_{011}t_{011}+c_{122}t_{122}$ (`general_identity` in [ClassTraceGeneral.lean](../lean/HamiltonsCubic/Results/ClassTraceGeneral.lean)), with $c_{122}<0$, $c_{011}<0$, and $c_{000}\le0$ iff $\lambda_0(2\lambda_1-\lambda_2)\ge\lambda_1\lambda_2$ (at $\lambda_1=0$ always; for $\lambda_1>0$ it needs $\lambda_2>2\lambda_1$ and $\lvert\lambda_0\rvert\ge\lambda_1\lambda_2/(\lambda_2-2\lambda_1)$). **On that region the certificate fires wherever $\mathrm{const}<0$**, with the same conditionality on the corrections; at $(-a,0,a)$ it is the statement above.

**What it does.** **On the symmetric family it excludes nothing** ([F78](fence-posts.md)): rows (A$_0$), (B$_0$), (C$_0$) give $4t_{000}+4t_{001}+t_{011}=(n-5-\kappa)p$, so with $t_{001}\ge0$ every point of the rows with nonnegative traces has $\kappa\le n-5$, which is [L244](#lemma-l244-the-per-vector-ceiling-on-a-symmetric-three-value-critical-point)'s $x$-free ceiling, and the band $\kappa>n-5$ holds no critical point (`band_empty` in [ClassTraceTest.lean](../lean/HamiltonsCubic/Results/ClassTraceTest.lean)). The statement is true and its hypothesis unmet. **The general extension is where it can act**: on the slice $\lambda_1=0$ its region is $\theta(\rho^3+2\rho^2-2)>\lambda_2\rho^2(n-2-2\rho-\rho^2)$, $\rho=\lvert\lambda_0\rvert/\lambda_2$ (`fires_l1_zero` in [ClassTraceSlice.lean](../lean/HamiltonsCubic/Results/ClassTraceSlice.lean)), and the rows' own ceiling $a\theta\le b^2(n-2)-2ab-a^2$ ($a=\lvert\lambda_0\rvert$, $b=\lambda_2$) confines it to $\rho>1$ (`slice_needs_rho_gt_one`), where the window $\rho^2R/(\rho^3+2\rho^2-2)<\theta/b\le R/\rho$, $R=n-2-2\rho-\rho^2$, is nonempty. It is conditional on the Ricci and $\Lambda^4$ corrections, which enter through a trace over the certificate space $U=\mathop{\mathrm{range}}(P_0C)^{\mathsf T}$; on the symmetric family the Ricci defect of $U$'s spanning vectors is carried by $E_1$ alone ([the note](../reviews/2026-09-25-I73c-per-vector-rows.md), `sym_split`, `comm_middle`; Gate 2 [r1175](../reviews/REVIEW_r1175.md), [r1176](../reviews/REVIEW_r1176.md)).

#### The maximiser's multiplication spectrum: what the moments give, and why they cannot close it

**The trace identities are [Fox's Theorem 1.3](#foxs-trace-identities-re-derived-here-and-the-conjecture-as-a-norm-minimisation), recorded below**, $\mathop{\mathrm{tr}}(L_x)=0$ and $\mathop{\mathrm{tr}}(L_xL_y)=\kappa_n\langle x,y\rangle$. *They have now been re-derived unwittingly three times in this project; the section below is the one that owns them.* **The constant is the part Fox leaves open** (his Remark 1.4, above $\dim V=4$):

$$\kappa_n=\frac{(n-1)(2n-1)}{4},$$

giving $9,\ 13.75,\ 19.5,\ 26.25$ at $n=5,\dots,8$. *It was first fitted to those four points and then checked at them, which is no derivation; two Gate 2 reviewers independently supplied the real one at every even $n$, by summing the six recorded Peirce families to $\sum\lambda^2=m^2(4m-1)/\big(4(m-1)\big)$.*

**Consequently the Peirce spectrum determines $\theta$:** $\theta^2\sum_\lambda\lambda^2=\kappa_n$, closed symbolically as an identity in $m$. *Whether this is genuine corroboration or circular is disputed and unresolved, one reviewer holds the six families independently derived from the Casimir, the other holds both sides to descend from the same eigensolver runs, and [algebra.md](algebra.md) records them as fitted with an origin supplied later.*

**A third moment exists in closed form**, so "only two moments" was never the obstacle:

$$\mathop{\mathrm{tr}}(L_x^3)=-\frac{5n-1}8\langle x^2,x\rangle+\frac{n(n-5)}8\langle x^{\#}x,x\rangle,$$
