confirmed **out of sample at $n=5$** to $10^{-15}$. With $a=\langle W^2,W\rangle$, $b=\langle W^{\#}W,W\rangle$ and $\theta=a+b$, it gives $S_3=\big((n^2-1)b-(5n-1)\theta\big)/(8\theta^3)$, the first moment to see $b$ at all.

**THE MOMENT METHOD IS DEAD, in one line.** A counterexample needs $\mathop{\mathrm{tr}}(L_e^2)<R(n):=\kappa_n/\theta_0(n)^2$, while every bound the maximiser conditions supply is a *lower* bound $L\le\mathop{\mathrm{tr}}(L_e^2)$. So $L<R$ holds **automatically at every multiplicity**, and the contradiction is unreachable. *What the method would need is a lower bound on $\mathop{\mathrm{mult}}(\tfrac12)$, which is equivalent to Conjecture A itself.* **The full moment problem fails too:** the localizing matrix for support in $(-\infty,\tfrac12]$ gives $\big[\tfrac{N-1}2+1\big]\big[\tfrac{S_2-1}2-(S_3-1)\big]\ge\big(S_2-\tfrac12\big)^2$, strictly stronger than any termwise bound, and it excludes no $\theta>\theta_0(n)$ at any $n$, with slack $1.93,2.61,3.36,5.09,7.12$ at $n=6,7,8,10,12$.

**DOWNGRADED to a heuristic, and it is the FOURTH telling of [fence post F6](fence-posts.md).** *Gate 2, reviewers 145 and 146, agreeing independently on every point below.* The algebra is right, with $S_2=\kappa_n/\theta^2$ and $S_3=\big((n^2-1)b-(5n-1)\theta\big)/(8\theta^3)$,

$$\tfrac12S_2-S_3=\frac{(n+1)\big[2n\theta-(n-1)b\big]}{8\theta^3},\qquad\text{since }(n-1)(2n-1)+(5n-1)=2n(n+1),$$

**and the inference drawn from it does not follow.** Three separate reasons:

- **The $n^4$ count measures the wrong thing.** A moment method here has no need to determine the spectrum; it must bound *one scalar*. And **one moment already determines $\theta$ exactly**, via $\mathop{\mathrm{tr}}(L_e^2)=\kappa_n/\theta^2$, so $S_2$ is pinned rather than free data, and a fibre dimension says nothing about whether a projection separates two sets.
- **Slack measured at feasible points is not evidence.** The ratios $1.93,\dots,7.12$ were evaluated at sphere products, where the conjecture *holds*. What a fence needs is failure at a would-be counterexample. Separately, $A_n\ge B_n$ with $\deg A>\deg B$ has a growing ratio for free, so the slack of an inhomogeneous inequality is not a measure of its strength.
- **The obstruction is DIRECTIONAL, not dimensional, and this file already states it four paragraphs above**, every maximiser condition on file constrains the same direction. That is [fence post F6](fence-posts.md) again: $e=W/\theta$ makes $\mathop{\mathrm{spec}}(L_e)$ and $\mathop{\mathrm{spec}}(L_W)$ the same set up to scale, so "spectral moments of $L_W$ are the wrong invariants" and this post are one statement. *[plan.md](../plan.md) already recorded three tellings of that failure. This is the fourth.*

**"And it holds at every localizing order" is FALSE as stated.** PSD at order $k$ is *every* principal minor, and the minor omitting index $0$ carries no $m_0\sim n^4$ entry, every entry is $\Theta(n^2)$, so its slack does not grow, which is exactly where the dilution mechanism is absent. **Orders $k\ge2$ were never available in any case**: $M_k$ requires $m_4,\dots,m_{2k+1}$, the record holds three moments, and this same file states that no fourth universal identity exists.

**What survives is a scale statement, and it needs no counting.** $\mathop{\mathrm{tr}}L_e^2=\Theta(n^2)$ spread over $\Theta(n^4)$ eigenvalues puts the spectrum of $L_e$ at scale $1/n$ while the fence sits at $\tfrac12$, giving $\mathrm{LHS}/\mathrm{RHS}=c^2/\overline{\lambda^2}$ directly. *That is the honest form: same exponent, no degrees-of-freedom argument, and immune to the objection above, but a restatement of the directional obstruction rather than a new fence.*

#### The gap theorem is FALSIFIED, and the Peirce table sits at a non-extremal point

**An ungraded critical point attains the floor exactly.** Padded $W_{\mathbb{CP}^2}$ at $n=5$ is critical to $3\times10^{-16}$ with $\theta=\sqrt{3/2}$, is ungraded, and has $\lambda_{\max}(L_e|_{W^\perp})=0.5000000000$. **So "ungraded $\Rightarrow\lambda_{\max}>\tfrac12$" is false**, and the target it defined survives only if restricted to $n\ge12$, a restriction that must be argued. *Found in a reviewer's log and verified independently.*

**The witness stands at EVERY $n\ge4$, but the $n\ge12$ restriction is UNTESTED rather than unavailable.** *Reviewer 164, adjudicating against [nondegeneracy.md](nondegeneracy.md)'s RESULT 1.* Padding preserves criticality with residual **exactly zero**: $\mathrm{pad}(W)$ already lies in $\mathrm{Weyl}_n$, and $\mathfrak{so}(n_0)$ being a subalgebra keeps $W^{\#}$ inside the block. The residuals $2\times10^{-1}$ and $8\times10^{-2}$ once recorded here came from a **zero-padded-then-projected** construction, and the projection is a no-op on a tensor already in $\mathrm{Weyl}_n$, so they measured the builder, not the point. *The withdrawn sentence was also self-refuting on its own terms: it granted criticality at $n=5$, and $4\to6$ factors through $5$. Four other places in the record compute the padded point at $n=6,7$ and find it well behaved.*

**The consequence drawn from this does NOT follow.** *Reviewer 166.* The target's hypothesis is about $\lambda_{\max}(L_e|_{W^\perp})$, and **padding transports criticality without transporting that spectrum**, this file records twice that padding *creates* degeneracy, $S^2\times S^k$ being nondegenerate natively and degenerate at every padding. $\lambda_{\max}=0.5$ for padded $W_{\mathbb{CP}^2}$ is on record at $n=5$ and $n=8$ **only, nowhere at $n\ge12$**. *So the escape hatch is untested, not closed, and it costs item 1 nothing either way, since [plan.md](../plan.md) had already replaced the dimension restriction with the **support** restriction, which padding at $n=12$ leaves intact.*

**The full Peirce multiplication table, computed for the first time**, at $S^3\times S^3$ ($n=6$, $\dim84$), spectrum $1^{(1)},\tfrac12^{(18)},\tfrac14^{(10)},0^{(30)},-\tfrac12^{(25)}$. *Gated on the rows it must get right: $e\circ x=\lambda x$ returns each block to itself at exactly $100\%$. An earlier version failed that gate, it indexed eigenvector labels into a basis-coefficient vector, and every row was wrong.* Four near-closures: $\tfrac14\times\tfrac14\to\tfrac14$ at $88\%$, $\tfrac14\times0\to0$ at $92\%$, $\tfrac14\times(-\tfrac12)\to-\tfrac12$ at $95\%$, $0\times(-\tfrac12)\to0$ at $81\%$, **averages over up to nine vector pairs, so possibly artefacts of basis choice inside degenerate blocks.**

#### The higher-variation calculus, and what it can and cannot see, Gate 2, Reviewers 287 and 288

**The contradiction the record carried here is resolved: the fusion rule does not track primitivity.** It is the **third-order necessary condition for a local maximum**, and its failure at $S^3\times S^3$ says exactly that $S^3\times S^3$ is not a local maximum of $P$, consistent with $\lambda_{\max}=\tfrac12$ there, because second order is *degenerate* at that point and third order is what decides. Both reviewers refuted the point directly from the definition, exhibiting a curve with $P(\gamma(t))>\theta$ tracking the $\sigma t^3$ prediction, while $\min\mathop{\mathrm{spec}}(\tfrac\theta2-L_W)|_{W^\perp}=-9.5\times10^{-16}$, **the second-order condition holds exactly and the third-order one kills the point**. That $S^3\times S^3$ is where this happens is not a coincidence: it is the *unique* degenerate sphere product, $c_B=-3(k-3)/(k-1)$ vanishing exactly at $k=3$.

**The jet of $P$ at a critical point, in closed form.** With $T(x,y,z)=\langle x\circ y,z\rangle$ fully symmetric, $W$ a unit critical point, $\theta=P(W)>0$, $L_W$ the multiplication operator, $B:=\tfrac\theta2-L_W$ and $A(\tfrac12):=\ker B\cap W^\perp$: for $z\in A(\tfrac12)$ of unit norm, $w\perp W$, and $\gamma=x/\lvert x\rvert$ with $x(t)=W+tz+\tfrac{t^2}2w$,

$$[t^3]\,P(\gamma)=T(z,z,z),\qquad [t^4]\,P(\gamma)=-\tfrac34\langle Bw,w\rangle+\tfrac32\langle z\circ z,w\rangle-\tfrac{3\theta}8 .$$

Both are exact, and the parametrisation loses nothing: the $t^3$ term of $x$ contributes $-6\langle Bz,v\rangle$, which vanishes because the orbit velocity lies in $\ker B$, *not by a cancellation between $P(x)$ and $\lvert x\rvert^{-3}$, and a radial component of $w$ is absorbed by rescaling. *Verified by both reviewers by hand and on independent code paths, and against generic random cubics with planted critical points.*

**The CHORDAL quartic, the coefficient in the parameter of $x=W+tz$, is $-\tfrac92\langle L_Wz,z\rangle+\tfrac{15}8\theta$**, hence $-\tfrac{3\theta}8$ on the whole null cone of $B$, not only on eigenvectors. **At $\theta>0$ it is negative whatever the algebra**, so the fourth-derivative *remark* accompanying Krasnov–Tkachev Prop 6.7 is satisfied strictly and carries no information; at $\theta<0$ it is positive and the test is decisive instead. *Prop 6.7 itself is untouched, its fusion rule comes from the **third** derivative.* This also settles the sign flagged in [the notes](../references/krasnov-tkachev-2018-idempotent-variety/notes.md): $\partial^4f/\partial z^4\vert_c=-9\theta\lvert c\rvert^{-4}<0$, so the printed $\ge0$ is the opposite convention.

**The orbit identity, which is what makes the apparatus vacuous along the orbit.** Differentiating $Q(e^{tX}\!\cdot\!W)=\theta\,e^{tX}\!\cdot\!W$ twice, with $z=X\!\cdot\!W$ and $u=X^2\!\cdot\!W$:

$$\boxed{\,B\,u=z\circ z\,}$$

and the first derivative gives $L_Wz=\tfrac\theta2z$, so **the orbit tangent lies in $A(\tfrac12)$ with no hypothesis at all**. Three consequences, none needing maximality: $T(z,z,z)=0$ on the orbit tangent; the fusion rule holds there, since $\langle z\circ z,y\rangle=\langle Bu,y\rangle=\langle u,By\rangle=0$ for $y\in\ker B$; and $u$ is the **stationary point** of the concave quartic functional, so where $B\vert_{W^\perp}\succeq0$ the supremum is attained there and equals $0$, i.e. $\langle b,B^+b\rangle=\theta/2$ **exactly**. *Stationarity is what forces this, not maximality, exhibiting one corrector of value $0$ would only bound the supremum below.*

**And the third-order content is the excess alone.** Applying the derivation to $L_Wz_1=\tfrac\theta2z_1$ gives $(X\!\cdot\!W)\circ z_1=B(Dz_1)$, so $T(X\!\cdot\!W,z_1,z_2)=\langle Dz_1,Bz_2\rangle=0$ for all $z_1,z_2\in A(\tfrac12)$. Splitting $z=o+e$ into orbit and excess parts, every term with an orbit factor dies and $T(z,z,z)=T(e,e,e)$. *The fourth-order quantity does **not** split this way, $\Pi(o\circ e)\ne0$, so the two orders behave differently.*

**Where this leaves route R8, stated as the reviewers corrected it.** The apparatus is vacuous on the orbit tangent at **every** critical point, unconditionally, so any use of Prop 6.7 must be shown to consume *excess* directions ($\dim A(\tfrac12)>\dim$ orbit tangent) or it is proving $0=0$. **The inference that it is therefore vacuous everywhere is refuted**, though not for the reason first recorded. Excess $=0$ **is** Morse--Bott nondegeneracy, which was already a theorem at every one of those points, so the measurement re-derived what was known and says nothing about a *degenerate* maximiser, and degeneracy is not excluded at a maximiser. *The structural argument offered alongside it, that a less symmetric maximiser carries more excess, is false and inverted: smaller isotropy gives a **larger** orbit tangent (up to $\tfrac{n(n-1)}2$ at trivial isotropy), which makes excess harder. The one point on the table with excess is $S^3\times S^3$, isotropy $6$ of $15$, among the most symmetric tested.* Excess is $0$ at $S^4\times S^4$, $S^6\times S^6$, $S^5\times S^7$ and padded $W_{\mathbb{CP}^2}$ at $n=8,10,12$, recomputed independently, matching $\tfrac{n(n-1)}2-\dim\mathfrak{isotropy}$, and $9$ at $S^3\times S^3$.

**The isotropy count says excess is non-generic, and that is as far as counting reaches.** Requiring $\dim\ker B=r+k$ beyond the automatic orbit kernel is $\sim\binom{k+1}2$ conditions and $T\vert_E\equiv0$ is $\binom{k+2}3$ more, while critical points are already isolated modulo the group, so a local maximum with excess is non-generic. **The count cannot be upgraded**, because $P$ is not a generic cubic and the points that carry excess are exactly the non-generic ones. *And no structural replacement is available: excess $0$ occurs at high symmetry ($S^4\times S^4$, padded $W_{\mathbb{CP}^2}$) and excess $9$ occurs at high symmetry too ($S^3\times S^3$, isotropy $6$ of $15$), while $\lambda_{\max}/\theta=\tfrac12$ holds at members of both groups. Neither symmetry nor the second-order ratio predicts excess.*

**Padding manufactures excess, and provably cannot manufacture a degenerate local maximum.** The record's statement that padding creates degeneracy is confirmed and quantified: $S^2\times S^4$ has excess $0$ natively at $n=6$ and $8,16$ when padded to $n=8,10$; $S^2\times S^6$ has $0$ at $n=8$ and $12,24$ at $n=10,12$. **But $\lambda_{\max}(L_W\vert_{W^\perp})/\theta$ is invariant under padding**, $0.6\overline6$ at every padding of $S^2\times S^4$, $0.6$ at every padding of $S^2\times S^6$, $\tfrac12$ exactly at every padding of $W_{\mathbb{CP}^2}$, because $\mathfrak{so}(n_0)$ is a subalgebra, so $L_W$ restricted to $\mathcal W_{n_0}$ is the native operator and the added directions never exceed its top eigenvalue. *Padding therefore never converts a non-maximum into a maximum*, and the one construction on record that reliably creates excess cannot produce the object route R8 needs. [run_padding_excess.py](../scripts/runs/run_padding_excess.py).

**And second-order maximality does not force excess $=0$**: $S^3\times S^3$ has $\lambda_{\max}=\theta/2$ exactly and excess $9$. What excludes it is the *third*-order condition. So the open question is sharp, **is there a critical point satisfying both the second- and third-order conditions with positive excess?** *Every local maximum on record has excess $0$; that evidence is drawn entirely from the symmetric catalogue and, per Reviewers 287 and 288, cannot settle the question.*

**The trace identities cap $\dim A(\tfrac12)$, and the cap can never force excess to vanish.** Fox's identities ($\mathop{\mathrm{tr}}(L_x)=0$, $\mathop{\mathrm{tr}}(L_xL_y)=\kappa_W\langle x,y\rangle$, $\kappa_W=\tfrac{(2n-1)(n-1)}4$, both reproduced here to $10^{-6}$ at $n=8,12$) give, at a unit critical point with $d=\dim A(\tfrac12)$ and $D=\dim\mathcal W_n$, by Cauchy--Schwarz on the $D-1-d$ remaining eigenvalues,

$$\frac{\kappa_W}{\theta^2}\ \ge\ 1+\frac d4+\frac{(1+d/2)^2}{D-1-d}.$$

**But the cap it yields is always larger than the orbit.** Dropping the last term, $d\le4(\kappa_W/\theta_0^2-1)$, and against the generic orbit dimension $\tfrac{n(n-1)}2$ the ratio is $\tfrac{(2n-1)n}{(n-2)(n-1)}$, which exceeds $1$ exactly when $n^2+2n-2>0$, every $n$, and tends to $2$. Measured with the full inequality: caps $59,146,265,597,1646$ at $n=8,12,16,24,40$ against orbits $28,66,120,276,780$, a ratio steady near $2.1$–$2.2$. **So the second-moment route leaves room for excess of the same order as the orbit itself at every dimension, and cannot close route R8.** *What would close it is an upper bound on $d$ below $\tfrac{n(n-1)}2$, and no trace identity of degree $\le2$ in $L_W$ can supply one.*

**A corollary of the fourth-order condition, derived and inert.** At a local maximum the fusion rule kills the $A(\tfrac12)$ component of $z\circ z$, so $\lVert z\circ z\rVert^2=\tfrac{\theta^2}4+\lVert b\rVert^2$; with $\langle b,B^+b\rangle\ge\lVert b\rVert^2/\lambda_{\max}(B)$ and $\lambda_{\max}(B)=\tfrac\theta2-\lambda_{\min}(L_W)$, the condition $\langle b,B^+b\rangle\le\theta/2$ gives, for every unit $z\in A(\tfrac12)$,

$$\lVert z\circ z\rVert^2\ \le\ \tfrac\theta2\big(\theta-\lambda_{\min}(L_W)\big).$$

**It decides nothing.** It holds with $12$–$25\%$ slack at every local maximum on record, and it fails to detect $S^3\times S^3$, $0.784$ against a bound of $0.833$, where the *third*-order condition refutes the point outright. So it is strictly weaker than the fusion rule wherever that bites, and slack where it does not. *Recorded so the corollary is not derived a second time: relaxing $\langle b,B^+b\rangle$ to $\lVert b\rVert^2/\lambda_{\max}(B)$ discards exactly the information that makes the fourth-order condition sharp.* **The apparent violation at the $C_5$ blow-up ($0.556$ against $0.375$) is not evidence**, the derivation assumes $B\vert_{W^\perp}\succeq0$, which fails there, so the inequality does not apply to that point.

**The object route R8 needs has three conditions, and every point on record fails one.** For the fourth-order inequality to be a live constraint at $W$ it takes $B=\tfrac\theta2-L_W\succeq0$ (second order), $\dim A(\tfrac12)>\dim(\text{orbit tangent})$ (positive excess), and $T\vert_E\equiv0$ (the fusion rule on the excess). *Both reviewers framed the missing object as the last two alone; $B\vert_{W^\perp}\succeq0$ is a third requirement and it is the one that fails at the best candidate.*

| point | $B\vert_{W^\perp}\succeq0$ | excess | fusion rule on $E$ |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| $S^4\times S^4$, $S^6\times S^6$, $S^5\times S^7$, padded $W_{\mathbb{CP}^2}$ | yes | **$0$** | vacuous |
| $S^3\times S^3$ | yes ($-9.5\times10^{-16}$) | $9$ | **fails**, $\max\lvert T\rvert=0.395$ |
| $C_5$ blow-up ($n=10$) | **no**, $\lambda_{\max}/\theta=1.206011$ | $20$ | holds, $\max\lvert T\rvert=1.9\times10^{-15}$ |

So the two points carrying excess each fail a *different* one of the other conditions, and every local maximum on record has excess $0$, where [post F37](fence-posts.md) makes the calculus an identity. **A local maximum with positive excess has never been exhibited**, and producing one, or proving none exists, is what decides route R8.

**The fourth-order inequality $\langle b,B^+b\rangle\le\theta/2$ is a theorem with no live instance.** It is necessary at each unit $z\in A(\tfrac12)$ *given* $T(z,z,z)=0$; wherever excess is $0$ it is an equality by the orbit identity, and at the one point with excess the fusion rule fails and the supremum is $+\infty$. **A critical point with positive excess and a satisfied fusion rule is what would first make it a constraint**, and none is known.

**But the table sits at a point that is not a local maximum.** Testing the *maximum* $\tfrac12$-mass over random pairs: $S^3\times S^3$ gives $31.9\%$ against $7\times10^{-31}$ at $S^4\times S^4$ and $2\times10^{-30}$ at $S^2\times S^4$. **The reason is now settled above**, $S^3\times S^3$ fails the third-order condition, so it is not a local maximum and the Peirce table there describes a saddle. **Any use of this table requires recomputing at $S^4\times S^4$ or higher.**

#### Lemma L51, the variation calculus is an identity along a critical orbit, the canonical statement

*Moved here from [results.md](../results.md) on 2026-08-31. The orbit identity and its consequences are derived earlier in this file; what follows is the full statement with its motivation, scope and sources, which existed only in that file.*

**Why it was needed.** *This lemma imports no theorem, the Argument below is self-contained, and the paragraph that follows is motivation rather than a citation.* The extremal-idempotent calculus of Krasnov–Tkachev is the setting it was found in, **and "applies verbatim" over-claims**: [fence-posts.md](fence-posts.md) records that $\mathcal W_n$ has no unit, and their *extremal* means maximiser where this lemma needs none. What is true is that critical points of $P$ on the unit sphere are idempotents of the metrised algebra whose cubic form is $P$, and a maximizer is an *extremal* idempotent. Its second-order consequence is Lemma L24. What is attractive is the third-order consequence, a fusion rule constraining *products of eigenvectors* rather than spectra, which is the kind of information every spectral route here has lacked. The question is what it actually constrains.

**Statement.** Let $T$ be a fully symmetric trilinear form on a Euclidean space, $P(x)=T(x,x,x)$, and $x\circ y$ defined by $\langle x\circ y,z\rangle=T(x,y,z)$. Let a compact group $G$ act orthogonally leaving $P$ invariant, let $W$ be a unit critical point of $P$ on the sphere with $Q(W)=\theta W$, $\theta>0$, and put $L_Wx=W\circ x$, $B=\tfrac\theta2-L_W$ and $A(\tfrac12)=\ker B\cap W^\perp$. Then for $X$ in the Lie algebra of $G$, with $z=X\!\cdot\!W$ and $u=X^2\!\cdot\!W$:

$$L_Wz=\tfrac\theta2z,\qquad B\,u=z\circ z .$$

Consequently the orbit tangent lies in $A(\tfrac12)$; $T(z,z,z)=0$ there; the fusion rule $\langle z\circ z,y\rangle=0$ holds for every $y\in A(\tfrac12)$; and **with $b:=\Pi(z\circ z)$ the fourth-order value at the stationary corrector $\Pi u$ is $\langle b,B^+b\rangle=\tfrac\theta2\lVert z\rVert^4$, giving $0$ exactly.** *The factor $\lVert z\rVert^4$ is not decoration: $z=X\!\cdot\!W$ is not a unit vector, and at an isotropy direction, where $z=0$, the unnormalised form returns $\tfrac\theta2$ for a constant curve.* **A witness settles it**: on $\mathrm{Sym}_0(3)$ with $P=\mathop{\mathrm{tr}}A^3$, $SO(3)$ acting by conjugation, $W=\tfrac1{\sqrt6}\mathop{\mathrm{diag}}(2,-1,-1)$ and $X=E_{12}-E_{21}$, every hypothesis holds and $\langle Bu,u\rangle=0$ checks exactly, **while the unnormalised display returns $[t^4]=\sqrt6/2>0$ on an orbit along which $P$ is constant.** The normalised one returns $0$. **None of this uses maximality**, and the equality needs no positivity either: it follows from $B(\Pi u)=b$, from $b\perp\ker B$, which is the fusion rule already proved, and from $[t^4]=0$ along the orbit, since $P$ is constant there. **Nor does it use compactness**, only that a Lie group acts orthogonally; and **$\theta>0$ is spent only as $\theta\ne0$**, three times, the sign being a normalisation. *Two hypotheses the argument uses and does not state: $X$ skew, twice, and finite-dimensionality for $B^+$.*

**$[t^4]=0$ IS TRUE AND ITS ARGUMENT IS ON FILE.** The value at the stationary corrector is $\tfrac34\langle Bu,u\rangle$, so the claim is exactly $\langle Bu,u\rangle=0$, a THIRD orbit identity, not implied by $Bu=z\circ z$ and self-adjointness together. **It follows from the infinitesimal invariance of $T$ alone.** $G$ preserves $\langle\,,\rangle$ and $\circ$, so $T$ is $G$-invariant, and differentiating $T(ga,gb,gc)=T(a,b,c)$ at the identity gives $T(Xa,b,c)+T(a,Xb,c)+T(a,b,Xc)=0$. At $a=b=c=z$ the three terms are equal by symmetry and $Xz=u$, so $3T(u,z,z)=0$. *No criticality, no fourth derivative, no skew-adjointness and no $\theta$: criticality enters only to identify $\langle Bu,u\rangle$ with $T(u,z,z)$, which is $Bu=z\circ z$ paired against $u$.* **The caution about the parabolic curve is side-stepped rather than met**: the $2$-jet does differ from the orbit at $O(t^3)$, and this argument differentiates no curve at all. *The fourth derivative of $P$ along the orbit itself also closes it, the $\theta$ terms cancelling as $3-12+9=0$, and that cancellation is FORCED, being $\tfrac32\theta$ times the fourth derivative of $\lVert w\rVert^2$, which vanishes by isometry* ([r985](../reviews/REVIEW_r985.md)). **Typed as `vanishing_from_invariance`, with the fourth-derivative route beside it as `pairing_vanishes`**, in [I53OrbitIdentity.lean](../lean/HamiltonsCubic/Results/I53OrbitIdentity.lean), standard axioms, no `sorry`. **Gate 2 PASSED** ([r984](../reviews/REVIEW_r984.md), [r985](../reviews/REVIEW_r985.md), identical cold briefs), *all eleven claims confirmed by both, and both found the short route independently of each other and of the parent.* **SCOPE**: established for $z$ TANGENT to the orbit, while the hypothesis $L_Wz=\tfrac\theta2z$ admits the whole $\tfrac\theta2$-eigenspace of $L_W$, which may be strictly larger, so the clause is about orbit directions rather than about every half-eigenvector ([r984](../reviews/REVIEW_r984.md)). **$B\vert_{W^\perp}\succeq0$ licenses only the word *supremum***, and it is strictly weaker than maximality: the cubic $P(x,y)=x^3+\tfrac32xy^2+\alpha y^3$ at $(1,0)$ has $B\vert_{W^\perp}=0$ and is no local maximum at $\alpha\ne0$.

**Argument.** $Q$ is $G$-equivariant, so $Q(e^{tX}\!\cdot\!W)=\theta\,e^{tX}\!\cdot\!W$ identically. Differentiating once at $t=0$ gives $2L_Wz=\theta z$. Differentiating twice gives $2\,z\circ z+2L_Wu=\theta u$, which is $Bu=z\circ z$. Then $\langle z\circ z,y\rangle=\langle Bu,y\rangle=\langle u,By\rangle=0$ for $y\in\ker B$, since $B$ is self-adjoint. For the fourth order, the exact jet along $x(t)=W+tz+\tfrac{t^2}2w$ with $w\perp W$ is

$$[t^3]\,P(x/\lvert x\rvert)=T(z,z,z),\qquad [t^4]\,P(x/\lvert x\rvert)=-\tfrac34\langle Bw,w\rangle+\tfrac32\langle z\circ z,w\rangle-\tfrac{3\theta}8\lVert z\rVert^4,$$

a quadratic in $w$, concave on $W^\perp$, whose stationarity condition is $Bw=\Pi(z\circ z)$, **satisfied by $\Pi u$ and not by $u$**, since $\langle u,W\rangle=-\lVert z\rVert^2$ puts the orbit acceleration outside $W^\perp$ and the corrector is $\Pi u=u+\lVert z\rVert^2W$. So the supremum is attained at the orbit's own second-order data, and equals $0$ because $P$ is constant along the orbit. $\square$

**What it does not reach, and this is the point.** The conclusion is *negative*: on the orbit tangent every conclusion of the calculus is an identity, so a route through it must consume directions of $A(\tfrac12)$ transverse to the orbit, the *excess*. Excess is $0$ at every local maximum on record ($S^4\times S^4$, $S^6\times S^6$, $S^5\times S^7$, padded $W_{\mathbb{CP}^2}$ at $n=8,10,12$), and the two catalogued points that carry excess each fail to be maxima for a different reason: $S^3\times S^3$ fails the third-order condition, and the $C_5$ blow-up fails the second. **A local maximum with positive excess has never been exhibited**, and until one is, this calculus constrains nothing.

*Setting the scope straight.* An earlier reading of this, that the calculus is therefore vacuous outright, was refuted at Gate 2 and is not claimed. Excess $=0$ is Morse--Bott nondegeneracy, **a theorem at three of the four points where it was measured**, the sphere products, by [Lemma L27](nondegeneracy.md), whose $p,q\ge3$ with $(p,q)\ne(3,3)$ covers $(4,4)$, $(6,6)$ and $(5,7)$. *At padded $W_{\mathbb{CP}^2}$ it is a measurement stopping at $n=10$*: [Lemma L28](nondegeneracy.md) is stated at $m\ge3$ in native dimension, and padding manufactures excess elsewhere, so those measurements re-derive what was known and say nothing about a degenerate maximizer.

**Also settled by the same computation.** The **chordal** quartic coefficient is $-\tfrac92\langle L_Wz,z\rangle+\tfrac{15}8\theta$, *the great-circle coefficient is $\tfrac78\theta-\tfrac52\langle L_Wz,z\rangle$, differing by $2\langle Bz,z\rangle$ and agreeing exactly on the null cone, which is the only place either is evaluated*, hence exactly $-3\theta/8$ on the null cone of $B$: at $\theta>0$ the fourth-derivative inequality accompanying the fusion rule is automatically strict and carries no information. This also fixes a sign left open in the source: $\partial^4f/\partial z^4\vert_c=-9\theta\lvert c\rvert^{-4}<0$.

**Sources and verification.** The second- and third-order statements are [Krasnov–Tkachev](https://arxiv.org/abs/1801.00617) Propositions 6.6 and 6.7; Proposition 6.6 is this document's Lemma L24, independently derived here before the source was found. The identity $Bu=z\circ z$, the exact jet, and the vacuity consequence are new. Gate 2 by two reviewers on identical briefs and independent implementations, both deriving the jet by hand and confirming every dimension.

#### Fox's trace identities, re-derived here, and the conjecture as a norm minimisation

**These identities are Fox's Theorem 1.3, already recorded in this file, and were re-derived unwittingly.** The section on Fox's algebra above states them outright, $\mathop{\mathrm{tr}}L(x)=0$ and $\mathop{\mathrm{tr}}(L(x)L(y))=\kappa_F\langle x,y\rangle$, together with the closed form for $\kappa_F$. What follows is therefore **confirmation by an independent route, not discovery**, and the cost of not checking the record first is one cycle. What is genuinely added here is the reformulation of the conjecture as a norm minimisation, the audited extension of $\kappa_W$ to $n=4\ldots13$, and the closure of the bound built on them.

**The derivation, kept because it is independent of Fox's.** $\mathrm{Weyl}_n$ is an irreducible $O(n)$-module, so any invariant element or invariant symmetric bilinear form built from the algebra is determined up to a constant, giving

$$\mathop{\mathrm{tr}}(L_x)=0\quad\text{for every }x\in\mathrm{Weyl}_n,\qquad \mathop{\mathrm{tr}}(L_xL_y)=\kappa_W\langle x,y\rangle,\qquad \kappa_W=\frac{(2n-1)(n-1)}4 .$$

The first holds because $\mathop{\mathrm{tr}}(L_x)=\langle x,\tau\rangle$ with $\tau=\sum_kB_k\circ B_k$ invariant, hence zero; the second because the left side is an invariant form on an irreducible module. Both identities are **Gate 2 landed**, audited independently by Reviewers 28 and 29 on identical briefs, who agree on every substantive point. $\kappa_W=(2n-1)(n-1)/4$ is exact across $n=4\ldots13$ between them, including every dimension the original claim left untested, and at $n=4$ it returns $21/4$, matching Fox's $21/16$ under his normalisation, so it **is** the Killing constant $\kappa_F(n)$, a value Fox's Remark 1.4 leaves open above $\dim V=4$. $\tau=0$ was checked in two unrelated bases with the individual summands $O(1)$, so it is cancellation rather than smallness, and $\mathop{\mathrm{tr}}(L_x)=0$ holds at random non-critical $x$ as well. $\mathcal W_n$ is absolutely irreducible of real type **already under $SO(n)$** from $n=5$ (commutant $=1$ at $n=5\ldots10$, with $n=4$ returning $2$ as a control), so the $O(n)$-versus-$SO(n)$ remark recorded with Fox's theorem is an $n=4$ statement only; for odd $n$ the two groups act through the same image on $\Lambda^2$.

**The first identity is machine-checked**, as `trace_leftMulS_eq_zero'` in [WeylTraceZero.lean](../lean/HamiltonsCubic/Results/WeylTraceZero.lean), on the standard three axioms. *The argument needs far less than irreducibility*: the Fox product and the trace are equivariant under every orthogonal $g$ ([WeylFoxEquivariance.lean](../lean/HamiltonsCubic/Results/WeylFoxEquivariance.lean), [WeylTraceInvariance.lean](../lean/HamiltonsCubic/Results/WeylTraceInvariance.lean)), so $x\mapsto\mathop{\mathrm{tr}}L_x$ is unchanged by averaging $x$ over the finite group of signed permutation matrices, and an element of $\mathcal W_n$ fixed by every sign flip and every transposition is zero ([WeylInvariantZero.lean](../lean/HamiltonsCubic/Results/WeylInvariantZero.lean)): the flips leave only $c_{ab}=R_{abab}$, the transpositions make $c_{ab}$ independent of $b$, and Ricci-flatness gives $(n-1)c_{ab}=0$. **The second identity is machine-checked too**, as `trace_leftMulS_comp` in [WeylTraceSchur.lean](../lean/HamiltonsCubic/Results/WeylTraceSchur.lean), with ONE constant for every pair, $\exists\kappa\,\forall x,y$, and without irreducibility: the operator $T$ with $\langle Tx,y\rangle=\mathop{\mathrm{tr}}(L_xL_y)$ is equivariant and self-adjoint, the diagonal elements $E_q$ ($+1$ on $\{a,b\},\{c,d\}$, $-1$ on $\{a,c\},\{b,d\}$) are its eigenvectors with one eigenvalue by their flip and permutation symmetries, and an element orthogonal to every rotation of every $E_q$ is zero, since the four-term relations with Ricci-flatness kill $x_{abab}$ and rational rotations then kill every other coordinate. The value $\kappa_W$ is not part of the Lean statement. *Lean fidelity and non-vacuity of the first: [r1116](../reviews/REVIEW_r1116.md), CONFIRMED.*

**Two further identities, offered by Reviewer 29 as repair.** First, $\kappa_W=\|T\|^2/D$ with $T$ the algebra's structure tensor and $D=\dim\mathcal W_n$, verified exactly as $315/35$, $1155/84$ and $3276/168$. **It is a corollary of the second identity, not an independent route**: summing $\mathop{\mathrm{tr}}(L_{B_i}L_{B_i})=\kappa_W\langle B_i,B_i\rangle$ over an orthonormal basis gives $\|T\|^2=\kappa_W D$ directly, by the total symmetry of $T$. So it restates the constant rather than proving it, and $\|T\|^2$ inherits the closed form $\tfrac{(2n-1)(n-1)n(n+1)(n+2)(n-3)}{48}$ from $\kappa_W$ rather than supplying it. **A proof still requires evaluating $\|T\|^2$ directly** as a contraction of the $\mathfrak{so}(n)$ structure constants against the Weyl projector, the route is real, but it is that computation, not this identity. Splitting $B_i\circ B_j$ into its symmetric and $\#$ parts does not help: the three pieces sum to $\kappa_WD$ exactly but are individually unpleasant, $\tfrac{385}6$, $\tfrac{245}3$, $\tfrac{1015}6$ at $n=5$, so the total is the clean object and the split is a dead end. The promising direction is representation-theoretic, since $\mathop{\mathrm{tr}}(L_xL_y)$ is the unique invariant form and $\kappa_W$ is therefore fixed by the Dynkin index of $\mathcal W_n$ as an $SO(n)$-module. Second, $\mathop{\mathrm{tr}}(X^\#)=-\|X\|^2$ on $\mathcal W_n$ at $n=5\ldots8$, the general form of the $\mathop{\mathrm{tr}}(W^\#)=-1$ constraint that killed the relaxation's witness.

**The $\tfrac12$-inclusion is proven, not measured.** Differentiating the equivariance $Q(gW)=gQ(W)$ along a one-parameter subgroup shows the orbit tangent lies in the $\tfrac12$-eigenspace at every critical point (Reviewer 29). The multiplicity can exceed the orbit dimension, $18$ against $9$ at $S^3\times S^3$, and $60$ against $21$ at $S^2\times S^3\times S^3$, but the inclusion itself is a theorem.

**The conjecture becomes a minimisation.** The second identity at $x=y=e$ gives $\|L_e\|_F^2=\kappa_W/\theta^2$, an identity holding at **every** unit element of $\mathcal W_n$, critical or not, since criticality never enters its derivation (Reviewer 28). On the critical set, where $\theta=P$, it reads

$$\theta^2=\frac{\kappa_W}{\|L_e\|_F^2},$$

verified as $\theta^2\sum\lambda_i^2=\kappa_W$ to the digit at every point tested. **Maximising $P$ over the critical set is exactly minimising the Frobenius norm of the multiplication operator**, with the spectrum partly pinned: $\lambda=1$ **at least** once, at $e$ itself, $\lambda=\tfrac12$ on **at least** the orbit tangent, and $\sum\lambda_i=0$. Both multiplicities can exceed those floors, $m(1)=\binom k2$ and $m(\tfrac12)=k\cdot\mathrm{orb}$ at $(S^3)^k$, and $m(\tfrac12)=18$ against an orbit of $9$ at $S^3\times S^3$, which is harmless for the bound, since it uses them only as lower bounds and the expression increases in each.

**The bound is not merely loose, it is the exact optimum of its moment problem, so the route is closed.** At a local maximum the second-order condition gives $\langle L_ev,v\rangle\le\tfrac12$ for every $v\perp e$, which is precisely the constraint the Cauchy–Schwarz minimiser already satisfies; the bound is therefore not a lossy relaxation and no moment-based sharpening exists (Reviewers 28 and 29, independently). Three escapes close with it: the third moment is proportional to $\langle x\circ y,z\rangle$ only at $n=4,5$, there being two independent cubic Weyl invariants from $n=6$, $\theta^2\sum\lambda^3$ is $0.3125$ at $S^3\times S^3$ against $-1.5104$ at $\mathbb{CP}^3$, same $n$ and same $\theta$; a forced kernel would need $\dim\ker\ge0.930\,D$ at $n=12$ and $0.999\,D$ at $n=100$ against a largest observed $0.714\,D$, and generic $L_x$ has no kernel at all; and the deficit is structural, $\kappa_W/\theta_0^2\sim n^2/4$ against a forced $\sum\lambda^2\sim\mathrm{orb}/4\sim n^2/8$. **No bound in this family drops below $\theta_0$ at any $n$**, with $\mathrm{bound}/\theta_0$ peaking at $1.5104$ near $n=10$ and tending to $\sqrt2$.

**The size of the gap, for the record.** Cauchy–Schwarz on the unpinned eigenvalues gives $\theta^2\le\kappa_W/[1+\tfrac{\mathrm{orb}}4+(1+\tfrac{\mathrm{orb}}2)^2/(D-1-\mathrm{orb})]$ with $D=\dim\mathrm{Weyl}_n$, which exceeds $\theta_0(n)$ by about $2.0\times$ at sphere-product orbits and $1.5\times$ at trivial stabilisers, stable in $n$. **It constrains symmetry-free points hardest**, which is the population no construction or search reaches, but to conclude $\theta\le\theta_0$ there the forced part of $\sum\lambda_i^2$ must rise by a factor of $2.2$ ($8.83$ against a needed $20$ at $n=8$; $18.2$ against $41.4$ at $n=12$), so what is missing is substantial rather than marginal.

**The spectrum does not determine the point.** At $n=8$, $\mathbb{CP}^4$ and $S^2\times S^6$ are distinct critical points, tensor overlap $0.3$, with **identical $L_e$ spectra** and the same $\theta^2=35/36$ (Reviewer 29). The coincidence is exact at $n=8$ and at no other dimension: $\theta^2_{\mathbb{CP}^{n/2}}$ and $\theta^2_{2,n-2}$ diverge from $n=10$ on. So the norm reformulation determines $\theta$, not the point, and a minimiser of $\|L_e\|_F$ need not be unique as a tensor. Both values sit below $\theta_0$, and $n=8$ is outside the conjecture's range, so nothing downstream moves.

**The moment method stops at two.** A third universal identity does not exist: $\theta^2\mathop{\mathrm{tr}}(L_e^3)$ takes the values $2.625$, $1.3125$, $-1.0$, $1.969$, $2.0$ and $5.906$ across the catalogue, so the invariant trilinear form $\mathop{\mathrm{tr}}(L_xL_yL_z)$ is not determined by the inner product alone, the non-associativity supplies a second independent invariant. Any sharpening must come from structure that pins eigenvalues, not from further moments.

#### Graded critical points: how far the classification reaches

**What the second-order criterion does and does not exclude, mapped.** Among two-block sphere products, every split with $p,q\ge3$ has $\lambda_{\max}$ **exactly** $\tfrac12$, measured at $n=8,9,10,12$ across all splits, while the $p=2$ family violates at excess $\tfrac1{2(n-3)}$. So the criterion does not single out the balanced product among graded points, and "$\lambda_{\max}=\tfrac12$ implies balanced" is false.

**$W_{\mathbb{CP}^2}$ is the first nondegenerate ungraded critical point on record.** It sits at $\theta=\sqrt{3/2}=1.2247449$, above $\theta_0$ at $n=8$ and $n=10$, below it from $n=12$ on, and is Morse--Bott nondegenerate at $n=8,10,12$, with $\dim E(\tfrac12)=\dim(\text{orbit})=\binom n2-4-\binom{n-4}2$, that is $18$, $26$ and $34$, **the single figure $18$ that stood here is the $n=8$ value quoted at all three.** The stabiliser is $U(2)\times SO(n-4)$, of dimension $4+\binom{n-4}2$, and the formula returns the record's own $10$ at $n=6$. It is therefore the shape any classification of critical points has to handle, and it is the same orbit Nienhaus's Theorem 4.19 localizes on.

**The ungraded margin is quantitative, not binary.** Ungraded points violate by a wide margin, excess $\tfrac14$ at the $\mathfrak{su}(3)$ bi-invariant, at $\mathfrak{so}(3)\subset\mathfrak{so}(5)$ and at $\mathfrak{so}(3)\otimes\mathfrak{so}(3)\subset\mathfrak{so}(9)$, $\tfrac13$ at the $\mathfrak{so}(5)$ adjoint, and $\tfrac12$ across every multi-block product, so $\lambda_{\max}\ge\tfrac34$ throughout. The graded violators, by contrast, have excess $\tfrac1{2(n-3)}$ and $\tfrac1{2(m+1)}$, which shrink to zero. Any argument here needs only a strict violation, and the observed margin is uniform.

#### Observation L25, every ungraded critical point on file fails the second-order bound, except on the $W_{\mathbb{CP}^2}$ orbit

Needed because the open half of the core is a statement about ungraded critical points, and this is the first criterion that speaks to all of them at once rather than to a structured family.

**Statement, at the populations named.** Across $155$ converged critical points at $n=6$, all ungraded, all with $\theta>0$, and every structured ungraded point on record, $\lambda_{\max}(L_e|_{W^\perp})>\tfrac12$ **except on the $SO(n)$-orbit of $W_{\mathbb{CP}^2}$ and its paddings, where it is exactly $\tfrac12$.**

> **THE UNQUALIFIED FORM IS REFUTED, AND THE WITNESS WAS IN THIS FILE.** *Gate 2, [r575](../reviews/REVIEW_r575.md) and [r576](../reviews/REVIEW_r576.md), by independent routes.* $W_{\mathbb{CP}^2}$ at $n=4$ is structured, on record, and called ungraded three times, [bounds.md](bounds.md) records $\dim E_+=1$, $\dim E_-=2$, $\dim\ker W=3$, so there is no $\mathbb Z_2$-grading at all. From $W=\mathrm{diag}(0,0,0,-1,-1,2)/\sqrt6$ its multiplication spectrum is $\{1,\tfrac12^{(2)},0^{(5)},(-1)^{(2)}\}$, so $\lambda_{\max}(L_e|_{W^\perp})=\tfrac12$, with $\mathop{\mathrm{tr}}L_e=0$, $\mathop{\mathrm{tr}}L_e^2=\tfrac72=\kappa_W/\theta^2$ and $\dim E(\tfrac12)=2=\dim\mathcal O$ as checks the construction had no freedom to pass, **and a fourth, that the multiplicities $1+2+5+2$ sum to $10=\dim\mathcal W_4$**, so the spectrum accounts for the whole space. **$\kappa_W$ here is Fox's constant**: $\theta^2=\tfrac32$ forces $\kappa_W=\tfrac{21}4=\kappa_F(4)$ for the $\kappa_F(n)=(2n-1)(n-1)/4$ of [nondegeneracy.md](nondegeneracy.md), so the relation is $\mathop{\mathrm{tr}}L_e^2=\kappa_F(n)/\theta^2$. *Checked in [L25.lean](../lean/HamiltonsCubic/Results/L25.lean).* **A second route is one line**: [conventions.md](conventions.md) gives $M(4)=\sqrt{3/2}=\theta(W_{\mathbb{CP}^2})$, so it *is* the maximiser and [Lemma L24](#lemma-l24-second-order-optimality-as-an-eigenvalue-bound-the-canonical-statement) forces $\lambda_{\max}\le\tfrac12$ outright. **And this file stated it in bold $124$ lines above**, for the padded point at $n=5$, at $0.5000000000$. *The exception is the one Conjecture A itself carries at $8\le n\le11$.*

> **AND THE RANGE $[1.17,\,96.3]$ IS FALSE UNDER EVERY READING.** The next sentence of this block lists $\tfrac34$, $\tfrac56$ and $1$, all below $1.17$, and the section preamble concludes $\lambda_{\max}\ge\tfrac34$ throughout. *$1.17$ is the floor of the $n=6$ solver population alone, and the sentence quantifies over a union.*

**What is worth stating instead**: $\lambda_{\max}(L_e|_{W^\perp})\ge\tfrac12$ holds at **every** critical point with $\theta\ne0$ (where $e=W/\theta$ is defined) from equivariance alone (*the eigenvector step, $T(A\!\cdot\!u,u,r)=\tfrac\theta2\langle A\!\cdot\!u,r\rangle$, is machine-checked on $\mathcal W_n$ as `orbit_half_weyl` in [OrbitWeyl.lean](../lean/HamiltonsCubic/Results/OrbitWeyl.lean), and $A\!\cdot\!u\ne0$ for some $A$ at every nonzero $u\in\mathcal W_n$ as `weyl_moves` in [OrbitFree.lean](../lean/HamiltonsCubic/Results/OrbitFree.lean), so that $\tfrac12$ is an eigenvalue of $L_e|_{W^\perp}$ at every unit critical point with $\theta\ne0$, `L24_sharp` in [L24Sharp.lean](../lean/HamiltonsCubic/Results/L24Sharp.lean)*), so \"fails the bound\" means *strictly*, and the content of this observation is that equality is attained only on the $W_{\mathbb{CP}^2}$ orbit. On the subalgebra family, where gradedness varies with no block count present, the values are exact rationals: $\tfrac34$ at the $\mathfrak{su}(3)$ bi-invariant and at $\mathfrak{so}(3)\subset\mathfrak{so}(5)$, $\tfrac56$ at the $\mathfrak{so}(5)$ adjoint, and $1$ across the multi-block products with $k\ge3$, where the value $1$ records that the idempotent is not primitive.

**What it does not say.** It is not a characterisation of gradedness. Two graded families violate the bound as well, $S^2\times S^{n-2}$ at $\tfrac12+\tfrac1{2(n-3)}$ and $\mathfrak u(m)\subset\mathfrak{so}(2m)$ at $\tfrac{m+2}{2(m+1)}$ **for $m\ge3$**, *the formula gives $\tfrac23$ at $m=2$ where the true value is $\tfrac12$, so without that restriction this clause asserts a violation at the very point that refutes the block*, the latter including $W_{\mathbb{CP}^3}$, which is graded and sits at $\theta_0(6)$ exactly while failing to be a local maximizer. The criterion detects non-maximality, and any attempt to make it an equivalence with $S>0$ is ruled out.

**Why it is nevertheless the live route.** Conjecture A needs only one direction: that an ungraded critical point with $\theta\ge\theta_0(n)$ cannot be a maximizer. Three candidate constructions of the violating direction are already eliminated, rank-two directions were said to be capped at $0$, *a claim with no support anywhere in [findings/](.)*, and $K$-invariant directions were said to be multiples of $W$, **which is false**: [diagonal-half.md](diagonal-half.md) records invariant directions reaching the full $\lambda_{\max}$. *All three eliminations trace to one paragraph in [ATTEMPTS.md](../ATTEMPTS.md) scoped to the subalgebra family.*

#### The $\mathbb{CP}^m$ family: nondegenerate at every $m\ge3$

**The $U(m)$-isotypic decomposition, which is published.** $\mathrm{Weyl}_{2m}\otimes\mathbb C$ is $U(m)$-**multiplicity-free** with ten summands, nine at $m=3$, [Howe–Tan–Willenbring §2.3.1](https://arxiv.org/abs/math/0311159), $GL_n\subset O_{2n}$ branching at $\lambda=(2,2)$, all multiplicity one for $m\ge4$; the count agrees with [Tricerri–Vanhecke (1981)](https://www.ams.org/journals/tran/1981-267-02/S0002-9947-1981-0626479-0/) once the three Ricci-and-scalar modules are removed. Since $e$ is $U(m)$-invariant, Schur makes $L_e$ a scalar on each summand, so each value is an **eigenvalue rather than a lower bound**:

| summand | dimension | $L_e$ |
|---|---|---|
| trivial, $=W$ | $1$ | $1$ |
| $\mathfrak{su}(m)$ adjoint | $m^2-1$ | $\frac{m+2}{2(m+1)}$ |
| $\Lambda^2E\oplus\Lambda^2\bar E$, **the orbit** | $m(m-1)$ | $\tfrac12$ |
| $V_{(1,1);(1,1)}$ | $\frac{m^2(m+1)(m-3)}{4}$ | $\frac{2}{m+1}$ |
| $V_{(2,1);(1)}\oplus\text{conj}$ | $\frac{2m^2(m^2-4)}{3}$ | $0$ |
| $V_{(2);(2)}$, Bochner | $\frac{m^2(m+3)(m-1)}{4}$ | $0$ |
| $V_{(2,2);\varnothing}\oplus\text{conj}$ | $\frac{m^2(m^2-1)}{6}$ | $-\frac{3}{m+1}$ |

Dimensions sum to $\dim\mathrm{Weyl}_{2m}$ and $\mathop{\mathrm{tr}}(L_e)=0$, both identically in $m$. **Both $V_{(2,1);(1)}\oplus\text{conj}$ and $V_{(2);(2)}$ carry $c_S+q_S^2=4(m+1)$, hence $L_e=0$, and the reason is the shared charge, not the trace.** *The trace identity forces only the single linear relation $d_5y_5+d_6y_6=0$ between the two rows, which a one-parameter family satisfies without either vanishing; it cannot separate them.* Against their dimensions.**

**Nondegeneracy, with no exception.** Setting each eigenvalue to $\tfrac12$ symbolically, only $\frac2{m+1}$ ever reaches it, at $m=3$, exactly where that summand's dimension $\frac{m^2(m+1)(m-3)}4$ vanishes. So $E(\tfrac12)$ **is** the orbit at every $m\ge3$, cleaner than the sphere products where $(3,3)$ is a genuine exception. Confirmed by measurement at $m=3\ldots7$: $\dim E(\tfrac12)=\dim(\text{orbit})=m(m-1)$, and $\theta^2=\frac{(m+1)(2m-1)}{3m(m-1)}$ exact to $m=12$.

**And it is not a maximiser**, which is why this closes a case rather than opening one: $\lambda_{\max}=\frac{m+2}{2(m+1)}=\tfrac12+\frac1{2(m+1)}>\tfrac12$, carried by the $\mathfrak{su}(m)$ adjoint, the recorded excess, now with its carrier named.

**The eigenvalues are derived, not verified** (Reviewers 42 and 43, identical briefs, agreeing; verified again here from the weights). $W_{\mathbb{CP}^m}$ has a closed form as a combination of the three $U(m)$-isotypic projectors of $\Lambda^2\mathbb R^{2m}$, with coefficients **rational in $m$** because the surd in $\theta$ cancels:

$$e=\frac{W}{\theta}=-\frac{1}{2m-1}P_{\mathfrak m}+\frac{m-2}{(m+1)(2m-1)}P_{\mathfrak{su}(m)}+\frac{2(m-1)}{2m-1}P_\omega,\qquad \theta^2=\frac{(m+1)(2m-1)}{3m(m-1)}.$$

Bianchi, Ricci-flatness and $\theta^2$ are proved symbolically for all $m$; the six $\sharp$ closed forms behind them are exact in GF(p) at $m=3\ldots20$. From $\sharp(vv^\top,X)=-\tfrac12D(v)XD(v)$, giving $\sharp(P_\omega,X)=-\tfrac1{2m}D(J)XD(J)$ and $\sharp(P_{\mathfrak u},X)=-\tfrac14[\mathrm{Cas}(X)+C_2X+XC_2]$, every block value collapses onto the $U(1)$ charge, so no mixing angle enters and

$$\lambda_S=1-\frac{c_S+q_S^2}{4(m+1)},\qquad c_S=\sum_i\nu_i(\nu_i+m+1-2i),\quad q_S=\sum_i\nu_i,$$

with $\nu$ the summand's highest weight as a $GL(m)$ weight. This reproduces all seven eigenvalues symbolically ([b_cpm_master_formula.py](../scripts/route-b/b_cpm_master_formula.py)).

**Nondegeneracy is then arithmetic.** $\lambda_S=\tfrac12$ exactly when $c_S+q_S^2=2(m+1)$. Across the seven summands that quantity is $0$, $2m$, $2(m+1)$, $4(m-1)$, $4(m+1)$, $4(m+1)$, $4(m+4)$: it holds **identically** on the orbit, and among the rest only $4(m-1)$ can meet it, at $m=3$ alone, precisely where $\dim V_{(1,1);(1,1)}=\frac{m^2(m+1)(m-3)}4$ vanishes. So $E(\tfrac12)$ **is** the orbit at every $m\ge3$. The critical row was reached three independent ways: the charge collapse above, a hand assembly $\frac{-(m+4)+3(m+1)+5(2m-1)}{3(m+1)(2m-1)}=\frac2{m+1}$, and explicit highest-weight vectors of weight $\varepsilon_1+\varepsilon_2-\varepsilon_{m-1}-\varepsilon_m$, exact in two primes at $m=3\ldots9$.

**One caveat on the pieces, which are compressions and not identities.** $\sharp(P_{\mathfrak u},\cdot)$ does **not** preserve $\mathrm{Weyl}_n$, $28$–$46\%$ of the image leaves, so per-summand values of the individual pieces, such as $\sharp(P_{\mathfrak u})=\frac{m-2}{6}$ on the orbit, are exact **as Rayleigh quotients** with eigen-residuals of order $1$. Only the assembled $L_e$ preserves $\mathrm{Weyl}_n$, which it does to $10^{-15}$.

**An anchor at the bottom of the family.** At $m=2$ the closed form returns $\theta=\sqrt{3/2}$ and $W=\mathrm{diag}(0,0,0,-1,-1,2)/\sqrt6$: Nienhaus's $W_{\mathbb{CP}^2}$ verbatim, so the family interpolates the literature's point at its lowest member.

**Three traps recorded because each cost a cycle.** $\mathfrak u(m)\subset\mathfrak{so}(2m)$ must be **solved for** as $\{E\text{ antisymmetric}:EJ=JE\}$: only $m$ elementary generators $E_{ab}-E_{ba}$ commute with $J$, spanning the maximal torus, and using them returns the torus's zero-weight space of dimension $m(m-1)$ in place of the true one-dimensional invariant line. And the invariant generator is defined up to sign, landing on the negative branch at $m=3,4,5,7$; $e=W/\theta$ is sign-invariant so no spectrum moves, but $\theta$ must be flipped before it is quoted.

**A projector that fixes $\mathrm{Weyl}_n$ can still be wrong.** Removing the $\Lambda^4$ part and then the Ricci part **is** the orthogonal projection in either order, totally antisymmetric tensors have vanishing traces, so Ricci annihilates $\Lambda^4$ and the removals commute. But a wrong normalisation in either step leaves a map that is still the identity on $\mathrm{Weyl}_n$, to $10^{-16}$, while being $15$–$30\%$ wrong on general input and not idempotent, a defect no test run on $\mathrm{Weyl}_n$ can detect. The instance here was a spurious factor $\tfrac12$ on the Kulkarni–Nomizu term. Because the broken map is also self-adjoint and fixes $\mathrm{Weyl}_n$, it preserves $\mathrm{Weyl}_n^\perp$ and appends junk rather than mixing, which is why summand eigenvalues survived it unchanged.

**Two convention notes worth stating once.** The index formula $(P_{\mathfrak u})_{ij,kl}=\tfrac12[\mathrm{Id}+J_{ik}J_{jl}-J_{il}J_{jk}]$ is the projection **only as the $(i<j,k<l)$ matrix**; contracting over all $k,l$ gives $2P_{\mathfrak u}$. And $\|\omega\|^2=m$. Separately, the inner product is $\tfrac12\mathop{\mathrm{tr}}(X^\top Y)$ at the $\Lambda^2$ level, where it makes $\{\phi_{ij}\}$ orthonormal, and the **plain Frobenius product on the pair matrix**. Applying the $\tfrac12$ at the operator level instead inflates every $\theta$ by $\sqrt2$. The repository's code and numbers use the correct convention throughout, anchored against $\theta_0(n)$ at the balanced sphere product and Nienhaus Proposition 2.25.

#### The crux, reduced: a value bound on the non-diagonal $S=0$ points

**The diagonal case is granted for even $n\ge12$, so $\{S=0\}$ splits and only one half carries value risk.** Whether a critical point is coordinate-diagonal must be measured as the **off-diagonal Frobenius mass minimised over $O(n)$**, not as the largest single entry in whichever frame the constructor used, the two statistics even disagree on ordering. Frame-minimal masses: $0$ for $S^p\times S^q$, the $C_5$ blow-up and padded sphere products; $0.837$ for $\mathbb{CP}^4$, $0.866$ for $\mathbb{CP}^5$, $0.707$ for padded $W_{\mathbb{CP}^2}$ ([b_diagonality_split.py](../scripts/lib/b_diagonality_split.py)). The classification is certified rigorously rather than numerically by the **Plücker obstruction**: a point is non-diagonalisable when some eigenspace of $W$ contains no decomposable bivector, and $\min\|\omega\wedge\omega\|$ is $0.61$, $0.63$ and $0.50$ at those three.

**The non-diagonal points are NOT enumerated by isotropy.** Unitary isotropy is sufficient for non-diagonalisability and **never necessary**. Padded $W_{\mathbb{CP}^2}$ is fixed by every single-coordinate reflection of the padding, each of odd $(-1)$-multiplicity, so its isotropy is not unitary while it remains non-diagonalisable, the $U(m)$ argument applies only in native dimension $4$. $\mathbb{CP}^m$ with $m$ odd fails likewise, complex conjugation sitting in the isotropy with multiplicity $m$. And $W_{\mathbb{CP}^2}\oplus W_{S^4\times S^4}$ at $n=12$ is critical to $7\times10^{-16}$ with $S=0$, frame-minimal mass $0.483$ and $\theta=0.8367$, matching no enumerated point. Since $\{S=0\}$ is closed under orthogonal direct sums, this manufactures an **infinite unlisted family**, five verified at $n=12$, all at $0.65$–$0.70\,\theta_0$.

**So the residual is a value bound, not a classification.** What route R2 needs is

$$\sup\{\theta:\ W\text{ critical},\ S(W)=0,\ W\text{ non-diagonal}\}\ \le\ \sqrt{3/2},$$

which closes every even $n\ge12$ by itself. Completeness of any enumeration is both stronger than required and already false, and an isotropy-indexed list can never reach the symmetry-free points. Every non-diagonal $S=0$ point known respects the bound: Fox $\mathcal S^4(\alpha)=$ padded $W_{\mathbb{CP}^2}$ at $\sqrt{3/2}$ exactly, which is $0.9909\,\theta_0(12)$ and the closest; $\mathfrak{su}(m)$ Kähler at $0.8290\,\theta_0(12)$, the degenerate family with excess $5,9,14,20$ at $m=3,4,5,6$; quaternionic-Kähler $\mathbb{HP}^2$ at $0.9737\,\theta_0(8)$, with $\mathbb{HP}^3$ leaving $\{S=0\}$ at $S=0.673$; and the $r\ge8$ hyperkähler family, a **six-dimensional** closed algebra matching Fox's $\mathbb B_r$ with exactly three $\theta$ magnitudes at each $r$, all three $S=0$ at $r=8$ and one of three at $r=12$, capped at $0.7483\,\theta_0(12)$.

**Two things the split does not do.** The grant is a statement about **values**, so it gives no openness at the diagonal $S=0$ points, and openness there remains owed, the $C_5$ corrector test being on record as blind. And $S=0$ is one grading condition of three, so Cartan's ceiling does not apply on $\{S=0\}$, only on the subalgebra family.

#### Circ-orthogonal splittings: the law's real hypothesis, and a proof of indecomposability

**The infinite direct-sum family is harmless for the value bound, and the reason is that its hypothesis was never "disjoint index blocks".** $Q$ is the quadratic form of the bilinear $\circ$, so $Q(aA+bB)=a^2Q(A)+2ab\,(A\circ B)+b^2Q(B)$ and $A\circ B=0$ alone makes $Q$ additive. Two consequences follow in two lines each, and neither was on record:

* **Frobenius orthogonality is implied, not assumed.** $\langle A\circ B,C\rangle$ is fully symmetric, it polarises the cubic $P$, so $\langle A\circ B,A\rangle=\langle B,Q(A)\rangle=\theta_A\langle A,B\rangle$. Hence $A\circ B=0$ with $\theta_A\ne0$ forces $\langle A,B\rangle=0$.
* **The harmonic-square law, on $\circ$-orthogonality.** Criticality of $W=aA+bB$ reads $a^2\theta_AA+b^2\theta_BB=\theta(aA+bB)$, so $a\theta_A=b\theta_B=\theta$ and $a^2+b^2=1$ give $1/\theta^2=\sum_i1/\theta_i^2$, hence $|\theta|\le\max_i|\theta_i|/\sqrt k$ for $k\ge2$ nonzero pieces.

**And $\circ$-decomposability is decidable by a rank computation.** If $W=\sum_ia_iA_i$ with the $A_i$ pairwise $\circ$-orthogonal and critical, then $A_i\circ W=a_iQ(A_i)=\theta A_i$, so **every $\circ$-summand lies in $E(1)$ of $L_e$**. Therefore $\dim E(1)=1$ *proves* $W$ admits no nontrivial $\circ$-splitting, it rules out splittings nobody has thought of, rather than failing to find one. $W$ itself always sits in $E(1)$, so a computed $0$ diagnoses a non-critical input; that guard fires on the naive $1/(p(p-1))$ multi-block diagonal, which is Weyl only for $k=2$ and has criticality residual $0.38$ at three equal blocks.

| point | $n$ | $\theta$ | $\dim E(1)$ | verdict |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| $S^3\times S^3$ | 6 | 1.054093 | 1 | indecomposable, proved |
| $S^6\times S^6$ balanced | 12 | 1.236033 | 1 | indecomposable, proved |
| $\mathbb{CP}^3$ | 6 | 1.054093 | 1 | indecomposable, proved |
| $\mathbb{CP}^4$ | 8 | 0.986013 | 1 | indecomposable, proved |
| padded $W_{\mathbb{CP}^2}$ | 12 | 1.224745 | 1 | indecomposable, proved |
| $C_5$ blow-up | 10 | 0.670820 | 1 | indecomposable, proved |
| $(S^4)^3$ | 12 | 0.829156 | 3 | splits |
| $(S^3)^4$ | 12 | 0.638285 | 6 | splits |
| $W_{\mathbb{CP}^2}\oplus W_{S^4\times S^4}$ | 12 | 0.836660 | 2 | splits |

**$E(1)$ is a subalgebra**, to $5\times10^{-15}$ leakage in every case tested, so the splitting can be read off by enumerating its idempotents. Doing so identifies the pieces exactly: $(S^4)^3=S^4\times S^8\;\oplus_\circ\;S^4\times S^4$ with $\theta=1.201561$ and $1.145644$ recombining to $0.829156$, and $(S^3)^4$ splits at $1.054093$ and $0.802044$ into $0.638285$, **the multi-block sphere products are $\circ$-decomposable across index blocks that overlap**, which is the "cross-block companion" the record predicted and never exhibited. Their recorded $1/\sqrt{k-1}$ value ceiling is a consequence of the law rather than a separate fact.

**What this settles and what it leaves.** Decomposable points are capped by their own pieces and can never set a new maximum, so Reviewers 56/57's infinite direct-sum family costs route R2 nothing: the residual narrows to **$\circ$-indecomposable** non-diagonal $S=0$ points. Padding is the $b=0$ case and preserves indecomposability, so padded $W_{\mathbb{CP}^2}$ at $\sqrt{3/2}$ stays in the residual as the sharp case. What the cap needs is $|\theta|\le M(n)/\sqrt2\le\sqrt{3/2}$, i.e. an *a priori* bound $M(n)\le\sqrt3$, strictly weaker than Conjecture A, since $\theta_0(n)<\sqrt2<\sqrt3$ at every $n$.

## Post F11, splitting $P$ into its two terms cannot deliver $M(n)\le\sqrt3$

**Fence post F11: splitting $P$ into its two terms cannot deliver $M(n)\le\sqrt3$.** At unit norm $\mathop{\mathrm{tr}}W^3\le1$, but the $\#$ term carries almost all of $P$ where $P$ is large: $\langle W,W^\#\rangle=1.2136$ at $S^6\times S^6$ against $\mathop{\mathrm{tr}}W^3=0.0225$, and it tends to $\sqrt2$ as $n\to\infty$ since $\theta_0\to\sqrt2$ with $\mathop{\mathrm{tr}}W^3\to0$. So the split can never bound $M(n)$ below $1+\sqrt2=2.414$. Any route to $\sqrt3$ must treat the two terms together. ([b_circ_indecomposable.py](../scripts/lib/b_circ_indecomposable.py))

#### The eigenvalue identity for $\langle W,W^\#\rangle$, and why flatness alone cannot cap $M(n)$

**In an eigenbasis of $W$ the sharp term is a cubic form in the eigenvalues with squared structure constants as coefficients:**

$$\langle W,W^\#\rangle=\tfrac12\sum_{\alpha,\beta,\gamma}\lambda_\alpha\lambda_\beta\lambda_\gamma\,c_{\alpha\beta\gamma}^2 ,$$

exact to $2\times10^{-16}$ at every catalogued point. It follows from $(W^\#)_{ab}=-\tfrac12\mathop{\mathrm{tr}}(A_aWA_bW)$ and total antisymmetry of $c$. What survives transporting $c$ to the eigenbasis is not its $0,\pm1$ entries but the two invariants $\|c\|^2=2(n-2)N$ and $\sum_{\alpha\gamma}c_{\alpha\beta\gamma}c_{\alpha\beta'\gamma}=2(n-2)\delta_{\beta\beta'}$, and those are what the bounds use.

**Two Cauchy–Schwarz arrangements then give a bound whose leading term is asymptotically exact.** With $y=\|\lambda\|_4^2$,

$$\langle W,W^\#\rangle\le(n-2)\,y,\qquad \mathop{\mathrm{tr}}W^3\le\|\lambda\|_\infty\le\sqrt y,\qquad\text{so}\qquad \theta\le\sqrt y+(n-2)y .$$

Since $y\ge1/\sqrt N$ always, the second term is at least $\sqrt2(n-2)/\sqrt{n(n-1)}\to\sqrt2$: **the bound cannot be improved by a constant factor**, and at the balanced sphere product it captures $0.97$ of the truth.

## Post F12, a single flatness inequality cannot cap $M(n)$; the flat and low-rank regimes need separate arguments

**But flatness alone cannot deliver $M(n)\le\sqrt3$, and the margin is not close.** The threshold $y_*(n)$ solving $\sqrt y+(n-2)y=\sqrt3$ is $0.1363$ at $n=12$ against the floor $0.1231$, about $20\%$ of room, and **six of the eight catalogued critical points exceed it**: $S^4\times S^8$ at $0.2195$, $(S^4)^3$ at $0.1759$, the $C_5$ blow-up at $0.2236$, $\mathbb{CP}^3$ and $\mathbb{CP}^4$ at $0.72$, padded $W_{\mathbb{CP}^2}$ at $0.7071$. Only $S^6\times S^6$ ($0.1251$) and $S^3\times S^3$ clear it. The bound's ratio to the truth tells the same story from the other side: $0.97$ at the balanced product, $0.12$ at padded $W_{\mathbb{CP}^2}$. **Fence post F12: a single flatness inequality cannot cap $M(n)$; the flat and low-rank regimes need separate arguments.** Unconstrained ascent from 60 starts at each of $n=6,8,10,12$ reaches $\sqrt{3/2}$ below $n=12$ and $\theta_0(12)$ at $n=12$, and nothing anywhere near $\sqrt3$. ([b_apriori_bound.py](../scripts/lib/b_apriori_bound.py))

#### A bound that survives both regimes, and the supremum it still owes

**Both halves of the flatness bound have a sharper replacement, and both are needed.** With $T=c'^2\ge0$ transported to an eigenbasis of $W$ and $\mu=|\lambda|$, a row Cauchy–Schwarz gives

$$\langle W,W^\#\rangle\le\tfrac12\sum_\alpha\mu_\alpha\langle T_\alpha\mu,\mu\rangle\le\tfrac12\sqrt{\textstyle\sum_\alpha\langle T_\alpha\mu,\mu\rangle^2}=:B_5 ,$$

and dropping the negative eigenvalues rather than bounding by the largest gives $\mathop{\mathrm{tr}}W^3\le\lambda_{\max}\,p$ with $p=\sum_{\lambda>0}\lambda^2$. At padded $W_{\mathbb{CP}^2}$ the first replaces $7.07$ by $1.00$ and the second replaces $0.8165$ by $0.5443$ against a truth of $0.4082$, **either alone still leaves that point above $\sqrt3$.**

$$\theta\;\le\;\lambda_{\max}\,p+B_5 .$$

**It clears $\sqrt3$ at every catalogued critical point**, worst case padded $W_{\mathbb{CP}^2}$ at $1.5443$: $S^6\times S^6$ $1.2933$, $S^4\times S^8$ $1.2843$, $\mathbb{CP}^3$ $1.3116$, $\mathbb{CP}^4$ $1.2420$, $C_5$ blow-up $1.0792$, $(S^4)^3$ $1.0984$, $S^3\times S^3$ $1.1729$. What low rank buys is the restriction of $c$ to the support of $W$: padded $W_{\mathbb{CP}^2}$'s support carries structure mass $4$ against $2(n-2)=20$. At the bottom the saving is exact, $c'$ is antisymmetric in three indices, so it vanishes identically on any subspace of dimension $\le2$ (checked to $6\times10^{-17}$ on random subspaces) and $B_5=0$ at rank $\le2$.

**The supremum itself now has a number, and it is dimension-independent.** With a *full* finite-difference gradient, affordable at $n=6$ ($D=84$) and $n=8$ ($D=300$), twelve ascents at each, one seeded at padded $W_{\mathbb{CP}^2}$ and eleven random, reach $1.544872$ and $1.545809$: $89\%$ of $\sqrt3$, and matching the $1.5449$ the seeded ascent reaches at $n=12$. **So the bound's supremum appears to be $\approx1.545$ independently of $n$**, against padded $W_{\mathbb{CP}^2}$'s own value $1.54433$. The maximiser is close to but not exactly a critical point of $P$: $\theta=1.2238$ at $n=6$ and $1.2223$ at $n=8$, both near $\sqrt{3/2}$, but full rank with criticality residual $2.7\times10^{-2}$ and $4.4\times10^{-2}$. **What is owed is the supremum as a theorem, not these values.** $M(n)\le\sqrt3$ needs $\sup_{\mathrm{Weyl}_n}(\lambda_{\max}p+B_5)\le\sqrt3$; subspace-gradient ascent *on the bound*, seeded at three catalogued points, raises it only to $1.5449$, so padded $W_{\mathbb{CP}^2}$ is a local maximum of the bound itself and not merely of $P$. **A naive random-direction climber reaches $1.03$ at $n=12$, below its own seeds, so it is a broken instrument and its numbers say nothing about the supremum**; only the seeded ascent is evidence. ([b_lowrank_bound.py](../scripts/lib/b_lowrank_bound.py))

#### Rank 3 is closed at every $n$, and the sharp structure constant is $\sqrt2$

**Three reductions narrow what $\sup(\lambda_{\max}p+B_5)\le\sqrt3$ has to prove.**

1. **$B_5$ is never worse than the flatness bound.** $\mu_\beta\mu_\gamma\le\tfrac12(\mu_\beta^2+\mu_\gamma^2)$ gives $f_\alpha\le(R\mu^2)_\alpha$ with $R_{\alpha\beta}=\sum_\gamma c'^2_{\alpha\beta\gamma}$ symmetric, nonnegative, row sums $2(n-2)$; hence $B_5\le(n-2)\|\lambda\|_4^2$. Worst observed ratio over the catalogue and 75 random $W$: $0.9748$. So the flatness bound can be dropped outright.
2. **At rank 3 the bound is a closed form in one structure constant.** A $3$-plane carries a single independent $c'$, namely $\kappa=\langle[v_1,v_2],v_3\rangle$, and $B_5=\kappa^2\sqrt{(\mu_1\mu_2)^2+(\mu_1\mu_3)^2+(\mu_2\mu_3)^2}$, exact to $10^{-16}$.
3. **$\max|\kappa|=\sqrt2$, at every $n$.** The Lie estimate $|\langle[x,y],z\rangle|\le\|[x,y]\|_F\le2\|x\|_{\rm op}\|y\|_F$ gives $2$; ascent over $3$-planes returns $1.414213562$ at each of $n=5,6,8,10,12$, attained at padded $W_{\mathbb{CP}^2}$'s own $3$-plane. **Random $3$-planes reach only $0.5634$**, a broken instrument for this constant, and recorded as one.

**Rank 3 is then closed, and the trace constraint is what closes it.** A Weyl tensor is totally trace-free, so $\mathop{\mathrm{tr}}_{\Lambda^2}W=0$ ($8.9\times10^{-16}$ across the catalogue) and a rank-3 $W$ satisfies $\lambda_1+\lambda_2+\lambda_3=0$ alongside $\sum\lambda^2=1$. Maximising $\lambda_{\max}p+2\sqrt{\sum_{i<j}(\mu_i\mu_j)^2}$ on that circle gives

$$1.544331,\qquad\text{at }\lambda=(2,-1,-1)/\sqrt6 ,$$

which is padded $W_{\mathbb{CP}^2}$ itself. **So every rank-3 Weyl tensor at every $n$ has $\theta\le1.5444<\sqrt3$.** Flat $|\lambda|$ would give $2/\sqrt3=1.1547$ from $B_5$ alone, and three equal magnitudes cannot sum to zero, the constraint is doing the work, not the estimate.

**What remains is rank $\ge4$.** The bound's observed supremum, $1.544872$ at $n=6$ and $1.545809$ at $n=8$, sits just *above* the rank-3 maximum, so the global maximiser is a full-rank near-miss of padded $W_{\mathbb{CP}^2}$ rather than the point itself. ([b_bound_structure.py](../scripts/route-b/b_bound_structure.py))

#### The bound's supremum depends on rank alone, and it closes $n=6$ outright

**Both terms of $\lambda_{\max}p+B_5$ see $W$ only through its eigenvalues and the subspace its eigenvectors span**, so the supremum can be taken over $r$-planes $V\subset\mathfrak{so}(n)$ and $\lambda\in\mathbb R^r$ with $\sum\lambda=0$, $\sum\lambda^2=1$, every Weyl constraint dropped except total trace-freeness. That set strictly contains $\mathrm{Weyl}_n$, so a supremum below $\sqrt3$ on it *proves* the bound.

| rank | 3 | 4 | 5 | 6 | 7 | 8 | 9 | 12 | 15 |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| $\sup$ | 1.544331 | 1.545949 | 1.547 | 1.5478 | 1.5485 | 1.5536 | 1.5631 | 1.6082 | 1.630017 |

**The profile cannot depend on $n$, and the reason is structural.** $\mathfrak{so}(n)\subset\mathfrak{so}(n+1)$ as a subalgebra with the structure constants restricting unchanged, so every $r$-plane in $\mathfrak{so}(n)$ is an $r$-plane in $\mathfrak{so}(n+1)$ and the relaxed supremum at fixed rank is non-decreasing in $n$. Measured, it is already saturated at $n=4$: rank 3 gives $1.544331$ at each of $n=4,5,6,8$ and rank 4 gives $1.545949$ at all four. So the profile is one universal function $\Phi(r)$ with $M(n)\le\Phi(n(n-1)/2)$, and the whole question becomes the growth of $\Phi$. It rises monotonically and slowly from the closed rank-3 value, and at $r=15$, which is *full* rank for $n=6$,

$$M(6)\le\Phi(15),\qquad\text{and }\Phi(15)\ \textbf{is not known to be}\ 1.6300 ,$$

> **THE INEQUALITY AGAINST $\sqrt3$ IS WITHDRAWN, the number was an ascent value, which bounds $\Phi(15)$ from BELOW.** *[ELIM-secondorder](../reviews/ELIM-secondorder.md).* The containment step is sound: $M(6)\le\Phi(15)$ holds because the $r=15$ set strictly contains $\mathrm{Weyl}_6$. But $1.630017$ is the best value an ascent *found*, i.e. $\Phi(15)\ge1.630017$, and nothing here bounds $\Phi(15)$ above, it could exceed $\sqrt3$. **"Closes $n=6$ outright" is unsupported**, and this file records in its own dual-error note the exact mistake committed here: an ascent value reported as a supremum. The optimum keeps padded $W_{\mathbb{CP}^2}$'s shape up to $r=7$, one eigenvalue near $0.83$, two near $-0.39$, the rest near zero, and switches at $r=8$ to a two-positive configuration $(0.71,0.45,\dots)$.

**Past $r=15$ the profile keeps climbing, and it converges too close to $\sqrt3$ to decide the question**: $1.6687$ at $r=24$, $1.6778$ at $28$, $1.6861$ at $32$, $1.6976$ at $40$. The increments decay, $0.0091$, $0.0083$, $0.0058$ per four ranks, so $\Phi$ does converge, but continuing that decay puts $\Phi(66)$, the value $n=12$ needs, at about $1.72$ against $\sqrt3=1.7321$: **under $1\%$ of margin, which numerics cannot settle.** So the relaxation closes $n=6$ and is too loose beyond it.

**The Weyl constraints are worth more than that margin, so they have to go back in.** At $n=6$ the relaxed full-rank supremum is $1.6300$ while ascent over $\mathrm{Weyl}_6$ itself reaches only $1.5449$, the constraints are worth $5.5\%$, against the $0.6\%$ the relaxation would have left. The Weyl-constrained supremum measured at $n=6,8,12$ sits at $1.5449$–$1.5458$ with $11\%$ of margin, and that is the object worth bounding.

**The relaxation is not merely loose, its optimum is nowhere near $\mathrm{Weyl}_n$.** At $n=6$ the full-rank relaxed optimum sits $56\%$ away from $\mathrm{Weyl}_6$ in relative Frobenius distance, and projecting it in drops the bound from $1.6302$ to $1.2374$, *below* what ascent inside $\mathrm{Weyl}_6$ reaches. Since the relaxed optimum's eigenvalues ($\lambda_{\max}\approx0.71$–$0.83$) already respect the Weyl-specific eigenvalue ceiling, $\max\lambda_{\max}$ over unit-norm $\mathrm{Weyl}_n$ is $\max_{\|u\|=1}\|P_{\mathrm{Weyl}}(uu^{\mathsf T})\|$, equal to $0.816497$ at $n=4,5$ and $0.843274$, $0.845154$, $0.881917$ at $n=6,8,10$, **the binding constraint is on the eigenvector planes a Weyl tensor can have, not on its eigenvalues.** That is what a proof has to use.

**And the Weyl-constrained supremum is flat in $n$.** Full-gradient ascent gives $1.544872$ at $n=6$, $1.545809$ at $n=8$, $1.546426$ at $n=10$, three independent starts agreeing to six digits there, and $1.5449$ at $n=12$ from the seeded ascent. Against $\sqrt3=1.732051$ that is $11\%$ of margin at every $n$ tested.

**A guard earned its keep here.** $\Phi$ is non-decreasing in $r$, a rank-$(r{-}1)$ configuration is a rank-$r$ one with a zero eigenvalue appended, so a lower value at higher rank is an ascent failure and not a value. A cold-start run reported $1.5510$ at $r=28$ after $1.6687$ at $r=24$; warm-starting each rank from the previous rank's optimum recovered $1.6778$. ([b_rank_stratified.py](../scripts/lib/b_rank_stratified.py))

#### The diagonal $S=0$ census, and why multistart root-finding cannot deliver it

**The diagonal subspace is small and explicit.** A diagonal $W$ has $W_{ijkl}=0$ unless $\{i,j\}=\{k,l\}$, so it is a symmetric matrix $w$ with zero diagonal; Bianchi is then automatic, every term of $W_{ijkl}+W_{iklj}+W_{iljk}$ being off-diagonal, and Ricci-flatness reduces to **every row of $w$ summing to zero**. So diagonal $\mathrm{Weyl}_n$ has dimension $n(n-3)/2$, $20$, $35$, $54$ at $n=8,10,12$. And **$S=0$ is triangle-freeness without assuming blocks**: the negative eigenspace is spanned by the $\varphi_{ij}$ with $w_{ij}<0$, and $[\varphi_{ij},\varphi_{jk}]=\pm\varphi_{ik}$, so the slack vanishes exactly when no triple has all three entries negative. That is the recorded $S_{\rm struct}=3\times(\text{triangles})$ with the block-constancy hypothesis removed.

## Post F13, multistart root-finding cannot census the diagonal $S=0$ set

**Fence post F13: multistart root-finding cannot census this set, and the reason is structural.** The $C_5$ blow-up is diagonal, exactly critical, triangle-free and block-constant with five blocks, so any honest census at $n=10$ must return it. It never does:

* power iteration on the projected gradient returns only *attracting* points, two-block sphere products and nothing else;
* root-finding returns dozens of low-$\theta$ solutions, $79$ distinct values under `hybr` and $54$ under `lm` from 200 starts each, the two solvers disagreeing about which solutions exist, and neither reaching $0.670820$.

The equation is right, $|F|=2.9\times10^{-16}$ at the $C_5$ point, which lies exactly in the diagonal subspace, but the point is **degenerate**, twenty excess directions, so the Jacobian is singular there and Newton cannot converge to it: seeded $0.05$ away it is recovered **$0$ times out of $20$**. So multistart root-finding systematically misses exactly the degenerate critical points, which are the ones a census is for.

**What works instead: enumerate over graphs.** $S=0$ is a combinatorial condition on the negative graph, so for each triangle-free $G$ seed $w$ negative on the edges and nonnegative off them, solve, and keep the solution only if its own negative graph is still triangle-free. Seeded on the $C_5$ blow-up's own $20$-edge graph at $n=10$, this **recovers $\theta=0.670820$ exactly**, the point root-finding could not reach from $0.05$ away and random-start `least_squares` missed in 150 tries. That graph carries $23$ distinct critical solutions, four with $S=0$: $0.670820$ and $0.612372$ on five blocks, $0.666576$ and $0.641091$ on six. **All four are block-constant.**

**At $n=6$ the census is complete and the answer is clean: every diagonal $S=0$ critical point is a padded two-block sphere product.** $5788$ triangle-free graphs give exactly four values, and quadrupling the seeds per graph from three to twelve produces **no new branch**, the same four, with hit counts rising from $90/90/875/1562$ to $348/350/3377/6458$:

| $\theta$ | identification | blocks | $\dim E(1)$ |
|---|---|---|---|
| $1.054093$ | $S^3\times S^3$ | 2 | 1 |
| $0.968246$ | $S^2\times S^4$ | 2 | 1 |
| $0.942809=2\sqrt2/3$ | $S^2\times S^3$ padded, one null coordinate | 3, one null | 1 |
| $0.866025=\sqrt3/2$ | $S^2\times S^2$ padded, two null coordinates | 3, two null | **2** |

The last splits, $\mathrm{Weyl}_4$ is self-dual $\oplus$ anti-self-dual and those two halves are $\circ$-orthogonal, which is why $\sqrt3/2=\sqrt{3/2}/\sqrt2$ is the harmonic-square value of two equal pieces. **No $C_5$ blow-up exists at $n=6$**, consistent with its negative graph needing five blocks that are connected, triangle-free and non-bipartite. The enumeration over graphs is exhaustive and the solve per graph is now twelve-seeded and stable, so the residual risk is a branch with a basin smaller than $12$ seeds reach.

**$n=7$ gives the same answer, over $133{,}500$ graphs.** Six $S=0$ values, all block-constant, none with singleton blocks: $1.095445$ and $0.979796$ on two blocks, and $1.054093$, $0.968246$, $0.942809$, $0.866025$ on three, the last four being the $n=6$ list padded. **Every diagonal $S=0$ critical point at $n=6$ and $n=7$ is a padded two-block sphere product.** The enumerator is validated by its own count: $5788$ and $133{,}500$ non-empty labelled triangle-free graphs, which are $5789$ and $133{,}501$ once the empty graph is added, the labelled triangle-free sequence exactly. ([b_diagonal_census.py](../scripts/lib/b_diagonal_census.py), [b_diagonal_graph_census.py](../scripts/route-b/b_diagonal_graph_census.py))

#### Bianchi is a linear constraint, and it closes 95% of the gap

**The first Bianchi identity is a condition on eigenvector planes.** It is the vanishing of the $\Lambda^4$ component of $W$ in $\mathrm{Sym}^2(\Lambda^2)$, and that component is the image of $v\otimes v$ under $v\mapsto v\wedge v$, so for $W=\sum_\alpha\lambda_\alpha v_\alpha v_\alpha^{\mathsf T}$

$$\text{Bianchi}\iff\sum_\alpha\lambda_\alpha\,(v_\alpha\wedge v_\alpha)=0\quad\text{in }\Lambda^4 ,$$

verified to $7\times10^{-16}$ across the catalogue against $0.545$ for a random traceless symmetric operator. This is exactly what the rank-stratified relaxation was missing: it couples the eigenvalues to the *planes*, which no eigenvalue inequality can see.

**It is linear, so it needs no penalty.** The feasible set is the kernel of the wedge map, the space of algebraic curvature operators, built once by SVD, with $\dim=n^2(n^2-1)/12$: $105$ inside a $120$-dimensional $\mathrm{Sym}^2(\Lambda^2)$ at $n=6$, $336$ inside $406$ at $n=8$, matching the formula exactly.

**The supremum over it is $1.548844$, identical at $n=6$ and $n=8$ to six digits.** Against the unconstrained relaxation's $1.630017$ and $\mathrm{Weyl}_6$'s $1.544872$, Bianchi closes $95.3\%$ of the gap and **Ricci-flatness accounts for the remaining $5\%$**, $1.548844\to1.544872$ at $n=6$ and $\to1.545809$ at $n=8$. That is $89.4\%$ of $\sqrt3$, so the margin is $11\%$.

**Two claims made from a penalty run are withdrawn.** Driving the residual down by raising $\rho$ stalls, and at $n=6$ it returned $1.544862$, under-converged, and taken at the time as agreement with $\mathrm{Weyl}_6$ to $10^{-5}$. So *"Bianchi closes $100\%$ of the gap, Ricci-flatness contributing nothing"* is wrong: it closes $95\%$. And *"Bianchi collapses the rank profile onto rank 3"* is wrong: the exact optimum is **full rank**, $15$ at $n=6$, $28$ at $n=8$, and beats the rank-3 value $1.544331$ by $4.5\times10^{-3}$. The profile collapses to *within* $4.5\times10^{-3}$ of rank 3, not onto it. A linear parametrisation and a penalty disagreed, and the linear one is the instrument kept.

**So the proof target is self-contained, with no Weyl machinery in it:**

$$\sup\Big\{\lambda_{\max}p+B_5\ :\ W\ \text{an algebraic curvature operator},\ \|W\|=1\Big\}\ \le\ \sqrt3 ,$$

a question purely about $\Lambda^2$ and its wedge into $\Lambda^4$. Its value is $1.5488$ at $n=6,8,10$, subspace dimensions $105$, $336$, $825$, and **Bianchi alone already carries the $11\%$ margin**, so the stronger conditions are not needed in the hypothesis. **The supremum is non-decreasing in $n$**, since padding preserves both the curvature-operator property and the bound ($B_5$ sees $c$ only on the support of $W$, where $\mathfrak{so}(n)\subset\mathfrak{so}(n+1)$ restricts $c$ unchanged), which is the guard that marks $n=10$'s other start, $1.4425$, as an ascent failure rather than a value.

**The ladder of constraints, at $n=6$ and $n=8$.** Trace-freeness is also linear, and adding it recovers about $42\%$ of what is left:

| constraint | $n=6$ | $n=8$ |
|---|---|---|
| none (relaxation, full rank) | 1.630017 | nothing recorded |
| Bianchi | 1.548844 | 1.548844 |
| Bianchi + $\mathop{\mathrm{tr}}W=0$ | 1.547170 | 1.547918 |
| Bianchi + Ricci-flat $=\mathrm{Weyl}_n$ | 1.544872 | 1.545809 |

**The extremal shape is stable across every row and both dimensions**: one large eigenvalue near $0.83$, two equal negatives near $-0.39$, and a small equal positive triple near $0.02$, a perturbation of padded $W_{\mathbb{CP}^2}$'s $(2,-1,-1)/\sqrt6$, which is $(0.8165,-0.4082,-0.4082)$. Rank 3 is closed in closed form at $1.544331$ and attained there, with Bianchi residual $1.5\times10^{-15}$; what the small triple adds is $4.5\times10^{-3}$. ([b_bianchi_constraint.py](../scripts/lib/b_bianchi_constraint.py))

#### Where $S=0$ says nothing, and what bounds $\theta$ there

**The slack has a one-line form in the eigenbasis.** $S$ is the part of $[\mathfrak m,\mathfrak m]$ landing back in $\mathfrak m$, and in an eigenbasis the bracket's components *are* the structure constants, so

$$S(W)=\sum_{\alpha,\beta,\gamma\,:\,\lambda<0}c'^2_{\alpha\beta\gamma},$$
