the structure mass carried by the negative eigenspace. It reproduces $S=0$ exactly at every catalogued $S=0$ point, and for diagonal $W$ it is the triangle count of the negative graph, the recorded $S_{\rm struct}=3\times(\text{triangles})$, with no block-constancy needed to state it.

**So $S=0$ is automatic on a whole stratum.** $c'$ is antisymmetric in three indices and therefore vanishes on any subspace of dimension $\le2$, giving $\dim\mathfrak m\le2\Rightarrow S=0$. **Padded $W_{\mathbb{CP}^2}$ lives there**, its spectrum is $(2,-1,-1)/\sqrt6$, exactly two negative eigenvalues, so route R9s *sharp* case sits precisely where the $S=0$ hypothesis carries no information.

**And that stratum's maximum is exactly $\sqrt{3/2}$.** Maximising $\theta$ over $\mathrm{Weyl}_n$ with the third-most-negative eigenvalue driven to zero, **seven starts out of seven** at $n=6$ and $n=8$ converge to $1.224745$, padded $W_{\mathbb{CP}^2}$'s value, to six digits. The penalty leaves that eigenvalue near $10^{-5}$, so this is the limit of a sequence approaching the stratum rather than a point exactly in it. **The maximiser is padded $W_{\mathbb{CP}^2}$ itself, identified by spectrum and not only by value**: $(0.81650,\,0\times12,\,-0.40825,\,-0.40825)$ at $n=6$, matching $(2,-1,-1)/\sqrt6$ to $10^{-5}$, with criticality residual $5\times10^{-5}$ carried by the penalty slack.

**The complementary stratum stays well below the sharp value.** Maximising $\theta$ over $\mathrm{Weyl}_n$ with $S$ driven to zero *and* the third-most-negative eigenvalue held below $-\delta$, so that $S=0$ is a real condition, gives $1.055973$ at $n=6$ and $1.131502$ at $n=8$, against $\theta_0$ of $1.054093$ and $1.145644$ and against $\sqrt{3/2}=1.224745$. The optimiser drives $\lambda_3$ to about $-0.2$ regardless of $\delta$, and the optima are strongly non-diagonal, off-diagonal mass $0.96$ and $0.98$. **Only one start in four and one in three cleared both constraints**, so these are lower bounds on the stratum's supremum, not the supremum. Taken together with the vacuous stratum:

| stratum | $S=0$ is | $\sup\theta$ found | $n=6$ | $n=8$ |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| $\dim\mathfrak m\le2$ | automatic | exactly $\sqrt{3/2}$, 7 starts of 7 | 1.224745 | 1.224745 |
| $\dim\mathfrak m\ge3$ | a real condition | lower bound only | 1.055973 | 1.131502 |

## Post F14, dropping Ricci-flatness is safe for the bound and unsafe for $\theta$

**Fence post F14: dropping Ricci-flatness is safe for the bound and unsafe for $\theta$.** The same maximisation run over the curvature-operator subspace instead of $\mathrm{Weyl}_n$ returns $\sqrt{2(n-1)/n}$, $1.290994$ at $n=6$, $1.322876$ at $n=8$, with $S=0$ exactly. That is the **round sphere** $W=I/\sqrt N$, for which $Q(I)=(n-1)I$; it has no negative eigenvalues and is not Ricci-flat. Since $\sqrt{2(n-1)/n}>\theta_0(n)$, $1.354$ against $1.236$ at $n=12$, relaxing to Bianchi alone would have manufactured a refutation of Conjecture A out of a tensor that is not a Weyl tensor. The relaxation that costs $5\%$ on $\lambda_{\max}p+B_5$ is fatal on $\theta$.

**Ascent on the criticality locus is blind too, and the control is one line.** Maximising $\theta$ over $\{M:\ M^{\circ2}=\theta M-M^2+\tfrac2nI\}$, the vertex-transitive rigidity locus, from $40$ starts tops out at $0.612372$ at $n=8$ against $\theta_0(8)=1.145644$, a feasible point it never reaches: **the balanced sphere product lies exactly on that locus**, rigidity residual $3\times10^{-16}$ and $\theta=\theta_0$ to twelve digits at $n=8,10,12$. So no bound on B1's $d\ge3$ branch can be read off such a run, and the control that establishes this is evaluating the known maximiser's residual before trusting the ascent.

**The $S=0$ ascent itself is blind and is recorded as such.** Constrained to $S=0$ over the curvature-operator subspace it reaches only $1.0605$ at $n=6$ and $1.1439$ at $n=8$, while padded $W_{\mathbb{CP}^2}$ sits at $1.2247$ with $S=0$ exactly, a feasible point $15\%$ higher that it never found. Those numbers are a lower bound on the supremum and nothing more. ([b_slack_constrained_max.py](../scripts/route-b/b_slack_constrained_max.py))

#### The diagonal $S=0$ points are not all blow-ups: circulants appear at $n=8$

**B1's owed claim is refuted, and the counterexamples are circulants on the $8$-cycle.** Running the census over the $410$ isomorphism classes of triangle-free graphs at $n=8$ turns up two $S=0$ critical points whose $w$ has **eight distinct rows**, no block structure at all, at $\theta=0.555683$ and $\theta=0.082270$. Both are exactly critical in $\mathrm{Weyl}_8$, residual $8\times10^{-16}$, with row sums exactly zero. Relabelling along the graph of the largest entry, which is a single $8$-cycle in both cases, makes $w$ **depend only on cyclic distance**:

| $\theta$ | $w$ at distance $1,2,3,4$ | negative graph |
|---|---|---|
| $0.555683$ | $0.2681,\ 0.0106,\ -0.1654,\ -0.2265$ | distances 3 and 4 |
| $0.082270$ | $0.2061,\ -0.1153,\ 0.2061,\ 0$ | distance 2 |

Both negative graphs are triangle-free, no two of $\{\pm3,4\}$ or of $\{\pm2\}$ sum to a third mod $8$, which is why $S=0$. **So blow-ups are the imprimitive case, and circulants are a second family**; the right invariant is constancy on the orbits of a vertex-transitive group, not a block partition. None appear at $n=6$ or $n=7$, where the census remains complete and returns only padded two-block sphere products. At $n=9$ the same two reappear as paddings, at the same $\theta$, among $16$ values in total.

**And at every $n$ censused the diagonal $S=0$ maximum is exactly the balanced two-block product**, $1.054093=\theta_0(6)$, $1.095445=\theta_0(7)$, $1.145644=\theta_0(8)$, $1.171080=\theta_0(9)$, which is the granted diagonal case appearing as an output rather than an assumption.

**Carried to $n=12$ by its own symmetry, the family stays harmless.** There are exactly $13$ triangle-free circulant supports on $C_{12}$, the distance sets $\{1\},\{2\},\{3\},\{5\},\{6\},\{1,3\},\{1,5\},\{1,6\},\{2,3\},\{2,6\},\{3,5\},\{5,6\},\{1,3,5\}$, and solving criticality on each returns a maximum of exactly $\theta_0(12)=1.236033$, which is the balanced two-block product itself. **The genuinely non-block-constant solution among them sits at $\theta=0.173439$**, with the rest ($1.217161$, $1.171080$, $1.145644$, $1.126872$) block-constant.

**This costs the classification and not the route.** At $0.5557$ and $0.0823$ against $\theta_0(8)=1.145644$, the new family is nowhere near the value that matters, the same shape as Reviewers 56/57's result on the non-diagonal side. **Completeness is the wrong target on both halves of $\{S=0\}$; a value bound is the right one on both.**

**The census over classes is what made this reachable.** Labelled edge sets are $133{,}501$ at $n=7$ and millions at $n=8$, while the isomorphism classes are $410$, $1897$, $12172$ at $n=8,9,10$; relabelling coordinates is an $O(n)$ symmetry, so one representative per class suffices. Neither nauty nor networkx is installed, so the generator builds class $n$ from class $n-1$ by joining a new vertex to an independent set, exactly the triangle-free condition, and deduplicates by spectral bucketing plus an explicit permutation search. Its control is its own output: it reproduces A006785 exactly through $n=10$. ([b_trianglefree_classes.py](../scripts/lib/b_trianglefree_classes.py), [b_diagonal_graph_census.py](../scripts/route-b/b_diagonal_graph_census.py))

#### $P$ is a Rayleigh quotient, and $\theta$ is an eigenvalue of its own matrix

**One line turns the cubic into a quadratic form.** In an eigenbasis of $W$, with $c'$ transported there,

$$P=\sum_\alpha\lambda_\alpha^3+\tfrac12\sum c'^2_{\alpha\beta\gamma}\lambda_\alpha\lambda_\beta\lambda_\gamma=\langle\lambda,M(\lambda)\lambda\rangle,\qquad M(\lambda)_{\alpha\beta}=\delta_{\alpha\beta}\lambda_\alpha+\tfrac12\sum_\gamma c'^2_{\alpha\beta\gamma}\lambda_\gamma ,$$

with $M$ symmetric because $c'^2$ is symmetric in its first two indices. Since $\|\lambda\|=1$,

$$\boxed{\ \theta\le\lambda_{\max}\big(M(\lambda)\big)\ }$$

**and at a critical point $\theta$ is already an eigenvalue of $M$**, not merely bounded by one: $Q(W)=\theta W$ is diagonal in the eigenbasis, so $M\lambda=\theta\lambda$ exactly. Across the catalogue $\lambda_{\max}(M)$ equals $\theta$ to six digits at every point except the $C_5$ blow-up, where $\lambda$ is not the top eigenvector, $0.809017$ against $\theta=0.670820$.

**Its supremum over $\mathrm{Weyl}_n$ is $\sqrt{3/2}$ at $n=6,8,10$** by full-gradient ascent, which is exactly $\max(\sqrt{3/2},\theta_0(n))$ there, since $\theta_0$ is $1.054093$, $1.145644$ and $1.200000$ there and padded $W_{\mathbb{CP}^2}$ dominates. From $n=12$ the ordering reverses. **If the supremum is $\max(\sqrt{3/2},\theta_0(n))$ in general, then for $n\ge12$**

$$M(n)\le\sup\lambda_{\max}(M)=\theta_0(n),$$

**which is Conjecture A**, with equality at the balanced sphere product.

**At $n=12$ random-start ascent under-converges, and the guard is a known feasible point.** It returned $1.224745$ again, while the balanced sphere product sits at $\lambda_{\max}(M)=\theta=1.236033$ and is feasible, so the true supremum there is at least $\theta_0(12)$ and the run is a failure to reach it, not a refutation. That matches the recorded basin statistics: padded $W_{\mathbb{CP}^2}$ captures $95$–$100\%$ of unconstrained ascent starts. What the run does establish is that nothing *above* $\sqrt{3/2}$ turned up outside the sphere-product basin.

**It is strictly the better object.** Over the same set $\lambda_{\max}p+B_5$ tops out at $1.5488$ and this at $1.2248$; both cost the same einsum. Where the earlier chain needed $\sqrt3$ and reached $89\%$ of it, this one lands on the conjectured value itself. ([b_operator_bound.py](../scripts/route-b/b_operator_bound.py))

---

*Re-filed here 2026-09-02 from [bounds.md](bounds.md) by the sort of [findings/](.). Conventions: [conventions.md](conventions.md).*

## The second-order form on $\mathrm{Sym}_0$: the averaging attempt, the isotypic branching, and V3
*From findings/bounds.md. Belongs in second-order.md because its subject is Lemma L24's $\tfrac12$ eigenvalue bound, the spectrum of $L_e$ on the symmetric deformations $H_A=D_AW+WD_A$, its isotypic block structure, and the V3 programme built on it, with $\Delta>0$ entering only as the hypothesis under which those directions are tested.*

**The averaging attempt: my closure was REFUTED, and what replaces it is stronger and points the other way.** *Gate 2, Reviewers 299 and 300 on identical briefs, agreeing.*

**Averaging does work. I had drawn the closure from the wrong subspace.** Over $\mathrm{Sym}_0$ itself the averages are exactly $7/17$, $3/5$ and $0.446362$ at $S^3\times S^5$, $\mathbb{CP}^4$ and the $C_5$ blow-up: **$3/5$ is above $\tfrac12$, not below**, so the argument already succeeds at $\mathbb{CP}^4$ and this record's sentence miscounted it. And over the **$C_5$-edge** sub-collection of the same $H_A$, the twenty $A=E_{ab}+E_{ba}$ with $a,b$ in blocks joined by an edge of $C_5$, the average is exactly $\tfrac23$, though the family is **not** an eigenspace. *Rebuilt here*: twenty generators spanning twenty dimensions, $L_e$ **constant** at $0.666666667$ across the whole span, orthogonal to $W$ and to the orbit tangent at $10^{-16}$. **So non-maximality at the $C_5$ blow-up is proved by averaging alone, with no eigenvector extracted.**

**A block-graph-refined average looked like it SEPARATED, and it does not.** *Reviewer 301.* The values are right, exactly $\tfrac23$ at the $C_5$ blow-up on a $20$-dimensional family, $41/76$ across all block pairs, exactly $\tfrac12$ at unbalanced two-block maximizers and at $K_k$ blow-ups, empty at balanced ones. **But an unpadded, kernel-free $n=9$ critical point kills it**: $\theta=0.641090782$, $\Delta=12.074836$, $S_{\mathfrak{mmm}}=0$, $\lambda_{\max}(L_e\vert_{e^\perp})=1.303584$, a genuine violator whose edge average is $0.472395$ and whose all-pairs average is $0.450411$, **both below $\tfrac12$**. Rebuilt here from the reviewer's vertex matrix, every figure matching to the digit. *And the $\tfrac12$ at unbalanced maximizers is forced rather than informative*, that family **is** the orbit tangent there, every principal cosine $1.000$, though the explanation does not generalise, since $K_k$ blow-ups and $S^3\times S^3\times S^3$ also give exactly $\tfrac12$ while being orthogonal to the orbit. **The statistic is also undefined at the conjectured maximiser and at this record's own $n=8$ witness**, neither having a block graph. So it is not a criterion.

**What survives, and it is a stronger closure than the one it replaces:** *no threshold on such an average can serve as a criterion*, because the ranges interleave. Across the fourteen distinct $\Delta>0$ points at $n=8$ the $\mathrm{Sym}_0$ average lies in $[0.330,0.382]$, while at the **maximizers** it takes $0.320$, $0.333$, $0.375$, $0.412$, overlapping. That kills every rescaled trace test rather than only the $\tfrac12$ one. *A near-miss worth recording so it is not retried*: deleting the ten-dimensional kernel of $L_e\vert_V$ at the blow-up lifts the average to $0.547808>\tfrac12$, and the manoeuvre dies at $n=8$, where that kernel is trivial at all fourteen points.

**And the lead is not narrower, it is a certificate.** $\lambda_{\max}(L_e\vert_{\mathrm{Sym}_0})>\tfrac12$ at **every one of the fifteen** $\Delta>0$ points on file, so $\mathrm{Sym}_0$ certifies V3 everywhere tested; $0.820541$ at the blow-up already closes that point, and the earlier inference that "a proof needs directions outside it" does not follow, a proof needs *a* violation, not the largest one. **The margin is thin somewhere**: at one $n=8$ point it is $2.5\times10^{-4}$ ($0.5002507$ against a full-space $0.568781$, re-solved to residual $1.7\times10^{-16}$). *Also corrected*: "the violating direction" is not well defined at either point, the top eigenvalue having multiplicity $15$ at $\mathbb{CP}^4$ and $2$ at the blow-up; and the blow-up's zero orbit component is a **theorem**, forced by $\mathcal O\subseteq E(\tfrac12)$ and self-adjointness, so it was evidence for nothing.

**REFUTED AS STATED, and repaired, $L_e$ is scalar on IRREDUCIBLES, not on isotypic components.** *Reviewer 301, mid-audit; recorded now because everything below rested on it.* An isotype of multiplicity $m$ carries an $m\times m$ block on its multiplicity space, and at the $C_5$ blow-up the **trivial** isotype is the counterexample: it is several copies of the trivial representation and $L_e$ is demonstrably non-scalar on it. **The reduction survives in repaired form**, block-diagonalise $\mathrm{Sym}_0$ by isotype, one $m\times m$ block per isotype rather than one scalar, so V3 is still a finite computation over the isotropy's representation theory, but the pieces are matrices.

**SETTLED: [Reviewer 301](../reviews/REVIEW_r301.md) is right, and the rival build omitted the Weyl projection.** *Resolved by running both against the definitions ([run_b1_trivial_isotype.py](../scripts/runs/run_b1_trivial_isotype.py)); the $L_e$ used passes the orbit-tangent control exactly, $\tfrac12$ to $10^{-16}$, at the blow-up and at $S^3\times S^5$.* **$H_A=D_AW+WD_A$ does not stay in $\mathcal W_n$**: at the blow-up every one of the $54$ directions leaves it, by $7.6\%$ to $50\%$ of its norm with median $32\%$. Computing $L_e$ on the raw $H_A$ therefore measures a different operator, which is what produced the five unrecognisable values. Projecting each $H_A$ back to $\mathcal W_n$ reproduces **the whole table below exactly**, $0^{\times10}$, $0.412281^{\times20}$, $0.441754^{\times2}$, $\tfrac23^{\times20}$, $0.820541^{\times2}$, and the torus invariants of $\mathrm{Sym}_0$ give $\{0.441754^{\times2},\,0.820541^{\times2}\}$, r301's four values, on the nose. Including the trace direction adds $A=I$, whose image is $W$ itself and whose eigenvalue is $1$: that is the radial direction [Lemma L24](second-order.md) excludes, and it is where the spurious fifth dimension came from. **So the numbers below are citable, with two conditions attached: the projection is mandatory, and the image is *not* $L_e$-invariant**, residual $1.245$ on the full $\mathrm{Sym}_0$ image even after projecting, **so these are compression values rather than eigenvalues.** The certificate use survives that unchanged, since a Rayleigh quotient above $\tfrac12$ bounds $\lambda_{\max}$ from below, which is the direction V3 needs.

**V3 reduces to a finite branching computation, and that is what the edge family was telling us.** The isotropy $\mathfrak k(W)$ acts on $\mathrm{Sym}_0(\mathbb R^n)$; the map $A\mapsto H_A=D_AW+WD_A$ is $\mathfrak k$-equivariant and $L_e$ commutes with $\mathfrak k$, so **$L_e$ acts as a scalar on each isotypic component that is multiplicity-free, and as a matrix on the multiplicity space otherwise**. Schur gives a scalar on an irreducible appearing once and an $m\times m$ block where it appears $m$ times, which is the distinction [L27](nondegeneracy.md) and [L28](nondegeneracy.md) both turn on. *Where the pieces below are multiplicity-free the values listed are eigenvalues*, which is why the $C_5$-edge family came out an exact eigenspace rather than a subspace with a large average. Measured, and the multiplicities are exactly the branching:

| point | $\Delta$ | $L_e$ on $\mathrm{Sym}_0$, value $\times$ multiplicity | above $\tfrac12$ |
|---|---|---|---|
| $S^3\times S^5$ | $0$ | $\tfrac14^{\times5}$, $\tfrac38^{\times14}$, $\tfrac12^{\times15}$ | **none**; the max is exactly $\tfrac12$ |
| $\mathbb{CP}^4$ | $0$ | $\tfrac35^{\times15}$, one piece | yes |
| $C_5$ blow-up | $12$ | $0^{\times10}$, $0.412281^{\times20}$, $0.441754^{\times2}$, $\tfrac23^{\times20}$, $0.820541^{\times2}$ | two pieces |

At $S^3\times S^5$ the pieces are $\mathrm{Sym}_0(\mathbb R^3)\oplus\mathrm{Sym}_0(\mathbb R^5)\oplus(\mathbb R^3\otimes\mathbb R^5)\oplus\mathbb R$, i.e. $5+14+15+1=35$, the last contributing nothing; at the blow-up the five pieces total $54=\dim\mathrm{Sym}_0(\mathbb R^{10})$. **The mechanism, exhibited at fixed $\mathfrak p$.** The $n=8$ witness has the *same* positive eigenspace algebra as $S^3\times S^5$, $\mathfrak p\cong\mathfrak{so}(3)\oplus\mathfrak{so}(5)$, and differs only in being non-equivariant. Its isotropy collapses and its branching refines accordingly:

| point | $\Delta$ | $\dim\mathfrak k(W)$ | isotypic pieces | $\max L_e$ on $\mathrm{Sym}_0$ |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| $S^3\times S^5$ | $0$ | $13$ | $3$ | $\tfrac12$ exactly, **at** the bound |
| the $n=8$ witness | $1.512$ | $\mathbf 5$ | $\mathbf{13}$ | $0.588721$, **above** it |

**The mechanism RUNS BACKWARDS at the extreme, and is refuted.** *Reviewer 301.* Small $\mathfrak k$ does not refine the decomposition, it **coarsens** it. Across $25$ diagonal classes at $n=8$, eight have $\dim\mathfrak k=0$, all with $\Delta\ge4.24$, and every one has exactly **one** isotypic component. The piece count runs $3\to11\to1$ as $\dim\mathfrak k$ runs $13\to5\to0$, so the middle term of "small isotropy, fine decomposition, a piece clears the bound" is false at both ends. *This record's own piece counts were wrong too*: $26$ rather than $5$ at the $C_5$ blow-up and $11$ rather than $13$ at the witness. **And branching never fixes values anyway**, the $25$ structurally identical two-dimensional weight components at the blow-up carry $0$, $0.412281$ and $\tfrac23$.

**A DERIVED identity for the deformation at any diagonal critical point.** For $A=\mathrm{diag}(a)$ the deformation has the closed form $N_{ij}=2M_{ij}(a_i+a_j)$ on curvature matrices, and substituting the diagonal criticality equation $M_{ij}^2+(M^2)_{ij}=\theta M_{ij}$ gives

$$(L_WH_A)_{ij}=(a_i+a_j)\big[M_{ij}^2+\theta M_{ij}\big]+2\big(M\,\mathrm{diag}(a)\,M\big)_{ij},$$

exact to $3\times10^{-16}$ at $S^3\times S^5$, $S^4\times S^4$, $S^6\times S^6$ and the $C_5$ blow-up, with $L_WH_A$ staying **diagonal**, so the whole computation closes inside the $n\times n$ matrix picture rather than on $\Lambda^2$. *This is a derivation and is unaffected by the refutations above.*

**The block closed form is DERIVED.** *Gate 2, Reviewers 301 and 302 on identical briefs, agreeing on all five claims; 302 supplied the proof the record owed.* Write the sphere product's Weyl part with $a$ on $pp$ pairs, $b$ on $qq$, $c=-s$ mixed; Ricci-freeness gives $a=qs/(p-1)$, $b=ps/(q-1)$ and criticality $\theta=(n-1)s$. For $A$ traceless in the first block, diagonalised there as $\mathop{\mathrm{diag}}(\alpha)$, $H_A$ is diagonal with $h_{ij}=2(\alpha_i+\alpha_j)\lambda_{ij}$; its Ricci is $-2a\mathop{\mathrm{diag}}(\alpha)$, and since $\mathrm{Ric}(D_E)=(n-2)E$ for trace-free $E$ the Weyl projection leaves the shape $\beta(\alpha_i+\alpha_j)$ on $pp$, $0$ on $qq$, $\gamma\alpha_i$ mixed, subject to $\beta(p-2)+q\gamma=0$. **That condition makes the shape one-dimensional, which is what forces scalarity**, and the $pp$ entry collapses to

$$\lambda=\frac{(p-2)(a-c)}{2\theta}=\frac{p-2}{2(p-1)} .$$

The mixed entry independently gives $[(n-2)(p-1)-q]/[2(n-1)(p-1)]$, and $(p+q-2)(p-1)-q=(p-2)(n-1)$ makes the two agree identically, *a consistency check the derivation had to pass*. Confirmed out of sample here at $(3,4)$, $(3,6)$, $(4,5)$, $(3,8)$, $(4,7)$, $(5,6)$, and at $p=2$ the predicted value $0$ shows up as **annihilation**, which is r301's identification of the unexplained $0^{\times10}$ at the $C_5$ blow-up, whose blocks have size $2$.

**And the other half of the claim is VACUOUS.** $L_e=\tfrac12$ on $\mathbb R^p\otimes\mathbb R^q$ because that piece **is** the orbit tangent, cosine $1.000000000000$ at every unbalanced pair, and orbit tangents are exact $\tfrac12$-eigenvectors at *every* critical point, which this record derives twice. **It would hold at a counterexample too.** So "no sphere product violates through $\mathrm{Sym}_0$" is a theorem but a narrower one than it reads: the only piece carrying information is $\mathrm{Sym}_0(\mathbb R^p)$, where $\tfrac{p-2}{2(p-1)}<\tfrac12$ simply because $p-2<p-1$. *Reviewer 301 widened it*: $\tfrac{p-2}{2(p-1)}$ holds at $S^3\times S^3\times S^3$, at $K_3$ blow-ups with blocks of $3$ and $4$, and at the $C_5$ blow-up itself, where $p=2$ gives $0$. With $\mathfrak k=\mathfrak{so}(p)\oplus\mathfrak{so}(q)$ acting on $\mathrm{Sym}_0(\mathbb R^n)=\mathrm{Sym}_0(\mathbb R^p)\oplus\mathrm{Sym}_0(\mathbb R^q)\oplus(\mathbb R^p\otimes\mathbb R^q)\oplus\mathbb R$,

$$L_e\big\vert_{\mathrm{Sym}_0(\mathbb R^p)}=\frac{p-2}{2(p-1)},\qquad L_e\big\vert_{\mathbb R^p\otimes\mathbb R^q}=\tfrac12\quad(p\ne q),$$

matching at $S^3\times S^5$ ($\tfrac14,\tfrac38,\tfrac12$), $S^4\times S^6$ ($\tfrac13,\tfrac25,\tfrac12$) and $S^3\times S^7$ ($\tfrac14,\tfrac5{12},\tfrac12$), **three points against a two-parameter guess, which would be a fit and not a proof, were the formula not already derived eight lines above.** *This clause is withdrawn: $\lambda_m=\frac{m-2}{2(m-1)}$ is derived, not fitted, and [Lemma L27](nondegeneracy.md) cites this file for the value.* *What a derivation must explain is the $q$-INDEPENDENCE*: the value is $\tfrac14$ at $p=3$ for $q=3,5,7$ alike, though Ricci-flatness ties the block values together as $x=-qz/(p-1)$, so $q$ enters the operator and must cancel. Set up and not finished; the weight $(M_{ij}+M_{kj})$ is constant on each block-column, which is where a proof would start. **Since $\tfrac{p-2}{2(p-1)}<\tfrac12$ for every $p$, no sphere product can violate through $\mathrm{Sym}_0$**, which is the consistency the route needed, now in closed form rather than observed.

**At the BALANCED products the mixed piece is annihilated, but by the WEYL PROJECTION, not by the deformation.** *Reviewer 301 refutes the statement as first written*: $H_A=D_AW+WD_A$ is **nonzero** there; what kills it is the projection into $\mathcal W_n$. The real content is an identity, $H_A=\tfrac1{n-2}\Phi(\mathrm{Ric}\,H_A)$ at a balanced product, residual $0$, *the deformation moves the curvature purely in the Ricci direction, leaving the Weyl part fixed to first order*, which is a sharper statement than annihilation and explains it. At $p=q$ only $\tfrac{k-2}{2(k-1)}$ survives, $\tfrac14^{\times10}$, $\tfrac13^{\times18}$, $\tfrac38^{\times28}$ at $S^3\times S^3$, $S^4\times S^4$, $S^5\times S^5$, because $H_A=0$ on all of $\mathbb R^k\otimes\mathbb R^k$ plus the trace split, $25+1$ of $54$ dimensions vanishing at $S^5\times S^5$. **So the conjectured maximiser is STRICTLY stable in the symmetric directions, with margin $\tfrac1{2(k-1)}$**, rather than merely saturating as the unbalanced ones do.

**So V3 becomes: show that some $\mathfrak k$-isotypic piece carries an eigenvalue above $\tfrac12$ whenever $\Delta>0$**, a branching computation over the possible isotropy algebras rather than a search over tensors. *And $\Delta>0$ is exactly the hypothesis that makes $\mathfrak k$ small, hence the decomposition fine and the pieces many*, which is [Reviewer 297's reading of how $\Delta$ should enter a proof.* Gate 2 owed.*

**No counterexample to V3 exists in anything searched**, the fifteen $\Delta>0$ points on file, plus $100$ and $58$ independently converged $n=6$ critical points from the two reviewers, $\lambda_{\max}$ reaching $96.291798$ and reproducing [Observation L25](second-order.md)'s recorded ceiling from code that never read it.

**And the GLOBAL first moment is worse than lossy, it is constant.** $\mathop{\mathrm{tr}}(L_W)=0$ identically on $\mathcal W_n$, which this file already records; measured again here at $10^{-15}$ at every catalogued point **and at non-critical ones**, so $\mathop{\mathrm{tr}}(L_e\vert_{e^\perp})=-1$ exactly, at every $W$ and every $n$, the raw traces $-0.012048\times83$, $-0.003344\times299$ and $-0.001300\times769$ all returning $-1$. *This is agreement with the record rather than a new fact*, but its consequence for V3 is new: **no global first-moment argument can distinguish a maximiser from anything at all**, since the quantity it would compare against $\tfrac12\dim$ does not depend on $W$. Together with the $\mathop{\mathrm{tr}}L_e^2$ budget's factor of two of slack, **the two GLOBAL moments are closed for V3**, though subspace averages are not, as the $C_5$-edge family above shows. *Both reviewers derived the identity rather than measuring it, and both note it is wider than stated*: $\mathop{\mathrm{tr}}_{\mathcal W_n}(L_Y)=0$ for **every** symmetric $Y$ on $\Lambda^2$, needing neither criticality, nor unit norm, nor $Y\in\mathcal W_n$, *but it does need the trace space to be Ricci-flat*, the same quantity coming out $+7.906$ over the Bianchi-only space. *A gap in the derivation was repaired*: invariance gives $\tau\in\mathbb R\cdot\mathrm{Id}$ rather than $\tau=0$, closed either by $\mathop{\mathrm{tr}}(x)=0$ on $\mathcal W_n$ or by $L_{\mathrm{Id}}\equiv0$ there, since $\mathrm{sharp\_bilin}(\mathrm{Id},Y)=-Y$ exactly. **And it is Fox's Theorem 1.3 reached for the fourth time**, so it is corroboration and not a finding.

**And the lead's scope is narrower than reported.** The violation is *entirely* inside the symmetric deformations at $\mathbb{CP}^4$, $\lambda_{\max}\vert_V=0.600000$ against the full-space $0.600000$, but at the $C_5$ blow-up they reach only $0.820541$ of the full $1.206011$. **So $\mathrm{Sym}_0$ captures the whole violation at the Kähler points and only part of it at the blow-up**, and a proof of V3 needs directions outside it too. *Note also that $\mathbb{CP}^4$ violates with $\Delta=0$*, which is the one-directionality again: whatever mechanism forces the violation at $\Delta>0$ points cannot be one that fires at $\Delta=0$ as well.

## The sign-flip decomposition, and the $4$-set block in closed form

*Gate 2, [r345](../reviews/REVIEW_r345.md) and [r346](../reviews/REVIEW_r346.md) on identical briefs. Both refuted the group the derivation used, both supplied the same repair, and both reached the $4$-set block's closed form independently of each other and of the brief; the two radicands are algebraically identical. Derivation at [DERIVE-df1.md](../reviews/DERIVE-df1.md).*

#### Lemma L120, $L_M$ is block-diagonal over the sign characters at every diagonal critical point

*Lean: proved on `CurvOp2` across the `L120*.lean` files ([L120Decomp.lean](../lean/HamiltonsCubic/Results/L120Decomp.lean), [L120Eigen.lean](../lean/HamiltonsCubic/Results/L120Eigen.lean) and their siblings): the split of every tangent vector at a $\Gamma$-fixed critical point into sign-isotypic blocks, only $\lvert t\rvert\in\{0,2,4\}$ carrying one, identified with $\mathcal D_n$, the pair and the four-set summands, $\dim(\mathcal D_n\cap M^\perp)=n(n-3)/2-1$, and `hess_blocks`, `eigen_blocks`: at $\theta>0$ L24's criterion holds on $W^\perp$ exactly when it holds on every block. Whether any clause of the statement is left untyped is under review ([r1773](../reviews/REVIEW_r1773.md)).*

**Let $\Gamma=\{\mathop{\mathrm{diag}}(\varepsilon_1,\dots,\varepsilon_n):\varepsilon_i=\pm1\}\subset O(n)$ be the full diagonal sign group. Then $\mathcal W_n$ decomposes as**
$$\mathcal W_n=\underbrace{\mathcal D_n}_{n(n-3)/2}\ \oplus\ \bigoplus_{\{i,j,k,l\}}\underbrace{V_{ijkl}}_{2}\ \oplus\ \bigoplus_{\{j,k\}}\underbrace{V_{jk}}_{n-3},$$
**and at every diagonal $M$, **criticality is unused; $\Gamma$-equivariance needs only that $M$ is $\Gamma$-fixed**, the operator $L_M$ preserves each summand. Hence the Hessian of $P$ on the sphere is block-diagonal in the same way, and [Lemma L24](#lemma-l24-second-order-optimality-as-an-eigenvalue-bound-the-canonical-statement)'s criterion is checkable block by block **once $\mathcal D_n$ is cut by $\langle M\rangle$**; the largest block a non-diagonal direction occupies has size $\max(2,n-3)$, and the $\binom n4$ four-set blocks have size $2$.

> **THE CUT IS NOT OPTIONAL, AND THE SENTENCE ABOVE IS THE ONE EVERY CITATION QUOTES.** $e=M/\theta$ lies in $\mathcal D_n$ and $L_ee=e$, so read without it the criterion gives $\lambda_{\max}(L_e\vert_{\mathcal D_n})\ge1>\tfrac12$ and **no diagonal critical point is a maximiser, vacuously**, the balanced sphere product included. **$W^\perp$ cuts $\mathcal D_n$ and nothing else**, since $M$ spans the trivial character and every other summand lies wholly inside $W^\perp$ by isotypic orthogonality. *And the block size fails at $n=4$ as first printed, where the four-set block is $2$ against $n-3=1$.* *Gate 2, [r507](../reviews/REVIEW_r507.md) and [r508](../reviews/REVIEW_r508.md) on identical briefs, both CONFIRMED and agreeing on every repair.***

**Proof of the decomposition.** On $\mathrm{Sym}^2(\Lambda^2)$ the element $e_{ij}\odot e_{kl}$ has character $\varepsilon_i\varepsilon_j\varepsilon_k\varepsilon_l$, and the overlap of $\{i,j\}$ with $\{k,l\}$ is total, one index, or none, giving the trivial character on the $\binom n2$ diagonal entries, the character $\varepsilon_j\varepsilon_k$ on the $n-2$ elements $e_{ij}\odot e_{ik}$, and a four-index character on the three pairings of a $4$-subset. Imposing the Weyl conditions: Ricci-flatness cuts the trivial block by its $n$ row sums to $\mathcal D_n$; first Bianchi cuts each $4$-set block from $3$ to $2$ by the single relation among the three pairings, **Ricci-flatness being empty there** since a trace needs a repeated index; one Ricci relation cuts each pair block from $n-2$ to $n-3$, **first Bianchi being empty there** since it needs four distinct indices. $\square$

**Proof of block-diagonality, and it needs no representation theory.** A support argument on the structure constants shows directly that $L_M$ preserves each $4$-set and each pair block at every $n$ ([r345](../reviews/REVIEW_r345.md)). *The equivariance route also works, but only with the full sign group*, and it is machine-checked: `L120_block_diag` in [L120Block.lean](../lean/HamiltonsCubic/Results/L120Block.lean), $L_M$ preserving every sign-isotypic subspace at a diagonal $M$.

> **THE DETERMINANT-ONE SIGN GROUP DOES NOT SUPPORT THE EQUIVARIANCE ARGUMENT, AND THE FAILURE IS EXACTLY AT $n=4,6,8$.** On $\Gamma\cap SO(n)$ the character $\chi_{[n]}$ is trivial, so $\chi_S=\chi_{S^{\mathsf c}}$ and complementary blocks **fuse**: at $n=4$ a $4$-set with the trivial block, at $n=6$ a $4$-set with a pair block, at $n=8$ two complementary $4$-sets into a single block of size $4$. With $\lvert S\rvert\in\{0,2,4\}$ a fusion needs $n\le8$, so nothing fuses at $n\ge9$. **The repair is free**, $\circ$ is $O(n)$-equivariant, so the full sign group serves and its even-subset characters are pairwise distinct at every $n$.

**The dimension identity $\tfrac{n(n-3)}2+2\binom n4+(n-3)\binom n2=\dim\mathcal W_n$ is transcription insurance and nothing more.** It factors as $\tfrac{n(n-3)}2\big[1+\tfrac{(n-1)(n-2)}6+(n-1)\big]$, tests the three block totals only, is blind to which relation cut each block, and **holds verbatim at $n=4,6,8$ where the isotypic claim fails**.

#### Lemma L121, the $4$-set block, its spectrum, and one necessary condition per $4$-subset

**Fix a **pair-diagonal** $M$ and a $4$-subset $\{i,j,k,l\}$, the block and its spectrum need diagonality alone, with no criticality and no row sum entering, which is what makes them applicable to $\mathop{\mathrm{diag}}(M)$ in a frame where $W$ is not diagonal. **The necessary condition below is the part that needs a maximiser**, through [Lemma L24](#lemma-l24-second-order-optimality-as-an-eigenvalue-bound-the-canonical-statement). Let $A=M_{ij}+M_{kl}$, $B=M_{ik}+M_{jl}$, $C=M_{il}+M_{jk}$ be the three opposite-pair sums. Then in signed coordinates $L_M$ restricted to the $4$-set space is $\tfrac12G$ with $G=\begin{pmatrix}A&C&B\\C&B&A\\B&A&C\end{pmatrix}$; **the Bianchi subspace is $\mathbf 1^\perp$** because $\hat a+\hat b+\hat c=0$ in these signed coordinates, and $G\mathbf 1=(A+B+C)\mathbf 1$ then makes that subspace **invariant**, two joints, not one. **The restriction to $\mathbf 1^\perp$ is traceless**; $\tfrac12G$ itself is not, its trace being $\tfrac12(A+B+C)$. Spectrum**
$$\pm\tfrac12\sqrt{A^2+B^2+C^2-AB-BC-CA}\;=\;\pm\tfrac12\sqrt{\tfrac12\big[(A-B)^2+(B-C)^2+(C-A)^2\big]}.$$

**The list is complete, not short a root.** $G$'s third eigenvalue is $A+B+C$ on the vector $\mathbf 1$, and first Bianchi is exactly $\mathbf 1^\perp$ in these signed coordinates, so that root is removed rather than missing. *And $G$ is symmetric: it is the Latin square with diagonal $(A,B,C)$ whose off-diagonal $(m,m')$ entry is the third letter, so $G_{12}=C=G_{21}$ and likewise for the other two. The signed coordinates are load-bearing, the raw block is $\mathop{\mathrm{diag}}(1,-1,1)$-conjugate to it, and that same flip turns Bianchi's $x_1-x_2+x_3=0$ into $y_1+y_2+y_3=0$.* *Gate 2, [r531](../reviews/REVIEW_r531.md) and [r532](../reviews/REVIEW_r532.md) on identical briefs, both CONFIRMED.*
**Consequently [Lemma L24](#lemma-l24-second-order-optimality-as-an-eigenvalue-bound-the-canonical-statement) gives, at every maximiser **that is coordinate-diagonal in the frame** and every $4$-subset, $A^2+B^2+C^2-AB-BC-CA\le\theta^2$.** *The frame hypothesis is not decoration: [L24](#lemma-l24-second-order-optimality-as-an-eigenvalue-bound-the-canonical-statement)'s $X\perp W$ is free exactly on the diagonal locus, which is where this inequality is available and nowhere else.* **Read without it, the sentence says the bound holds in every frame**, and a plan item was closed on that reading before two reviewers refuted it, the nesting running the other way round.

**What is new here and what is agreement.** *Agreement*: at $S^p\times S^q$ only the $(2,2)$-split $4$-subsets are nonzero and the value is $\tfrac{n-2}{2(p-1)(q-1)}$, which is **[Lemma L26](nondegeneracy.md)**, so the closed form at sphere products duplicates the record. *New*: that L26's dangerous summand **is** the $4$-set block, the extension from sphere products to an arbitrary diagonal $M$, the second eigenvalue $-\mu$, and the per-$4$-subset inequality above.

**It explains a recorded coincidence.** This file reports $\dim E(\tfrac12)=18$ at $S^3\times S^3$ against a $9$-dimensional orbit and calls the remaining nine directions *an eigenvalue coincidence with no group origin*. **They are the nine $(2,2)$-split $4$-subsets**, $\binom32^2=9$ of them, each contributing one $+\tfrac12$ eigenvector, since $\mu=\tfrac{n-2}{2(p-1)(q-1)}=\tfrac2{n-2}=\tfrac12$ at $n=6$. *The coincidence has a group origin after all.* Likewise the padding-invariant values $\tfrac23$ at $S^2\times S^4$ and $\tfrac35$ at $S^2\times S^6$ are $4$-set eigenvalues, so their padding-invariance is structural.

**And it settles an attribution dispute in both directions.** The value $(p-2)/(2(p-1))$ **(an $L_e$ value; the paragraph above it is in $L_M$ units)** tops each within-factor **pair** block *and* occurs inside $\mathcal D_n$; the record's attribution to the diagonal restriction and the screen that found it off the diagonal are both correct.

#### Lemma L122, the pair block is a graph Laplacian of the sectional matrix

**Let $M$ be a coordinate-diagonal Weyl operator with sectional matrix $M_{ab}$, and fix a pair $\{j,k\}$. In the basis $f_i=e_{ji}\odot e_{ki}$, $i\notin\{j,k\}$, the pair block of $L_M$ is**
$$L\big|_{\{j,k\}}\;=\;\tfrac12\Big[\mathop{\mathrm{diag}}\big(M_{ji}+M_{ki}\big)-M_{jk}\,\mathrm{Id}+M^{(jk)}\Big]\;=\;-\tfrac12\Delta^{(jk)}-\tfrac12M_{jk}\,\mathrm{Id},$$
**where $M^{(jk)}$ is the hollow submatrix of $M$ on the indices other than $j,k$, and $\Delta^{(jk)}$ is the Kirchhoff matrix of *that deleted matrix*, $\mathop{\mathrm{diag}}(\text{its row sums})-M^{(jk)}$, with degrees $-(M_{ij}+M_{ik})$. The Ricci cut on this block is exactly $\sum_ix_i=0$, that matrix's own $\mathbf 1^\perp$.**

**The three claims here have three different hypotheses.** The bracketed form needs **diagonality alone**, not criticality, not $\theta>0$, not Ricci-flatness. The Laplacian rewriting needs in addition $\sum_bM_{ib}=0$ for every $i\notin\{j,k\}$, which is $n-2$ of the $n$ Ricci conditions, rows $j$ and $k$ never entering. **And the inequality below needs $M$ to be a maximiser**, since that is what [Lemma L24](#lemma-l24-second-order-optimality-as-an-eigenvalue-bound-the-canonical-statement) requires.

> **READ WITHOUT THE MAXIMISER HYPOTHESIS THE INEQUALITY IS FALSE, AND [POST F48](#post-f48-lambda_maxwidetilde-nlethetam_jk-is-false-at-padded-pairs-and-it-was-never-what-the-pair-clause-asked) BELOW SAYS SO.** Pad $S^6\times S^6$ to $n=14$ and take the two padded indices as the pair: Ricci-flatness gives $5\alpha+6\gamma=0$, so the $\mathbf1^\perp$ eigenvalue is $5\alpha-6\gamma=-12\gamma$ against a floor of $-\theta=-11\gamma$. That point is coordinate-diagonal, Ricci-flat, critical and has $\theta>0$, every hypothesis the first version listed, and is **not a maximiser**. Restricting to $\mathbf1^\perp$ does not rescue it. *Gate 2, [r509](../reviews/REVIEW_r509.md) and [r510](../reviews/REVIEW_r510.md) on identical briefs, agreeing.*
>
> **And "$\Delta^{(jk)}$ is the Laplacian of $M$ with rows and columns deleted" was ambiguous in the direction that makes the identity false**: read as the submatrix of the full Laplacian it loses the whole $\mathop{\mathrm{diag}}(M_{ji}+M_{ki})$ term. **"Graph Laplacian" also overreaches**, the weights are signed, $\mathop{\mathrm{tr}}\Delta^{(jk)}=2M_{jk}$ forces a negative eigenvalue when $M_{jk}<0$, and the lemma's own content is that $\lambda_{\min}<0$; only symmetry and $\Delta\mathbf1=0$ are used, so Matrix-Tree, Cheeger and Perron do not transfer.
>
> *The basis $f_i$ is orthogonal of common norm $\sqrt2$ rather than orthonormal. No factor is owed, the scaling being uniform, and the lemma had been silent on it.*

**Consequently [Lemma L24](#lemma-l24-second-order-optimality-as-an-eigenvalue-bound-the-canonical-statement) on the non-diagonal directions is a spectral condition on these Kirchhoff matrices, at every coordinate-diagonal *maximiser*,** one per pair:
$$\lambda_{\min}\Big(\Delta^{(jk)}\Big|_{\mathbf 1^\perp}\Big)\;\ge\;-\big(\theta+M_{jk}\big)\qquad\text{for every pair }\{j,k\}.$$

*Gate 2, [r351](../reviews/REVIEW_r351.md) and [r352](../reviews/REVIEW_r352.md) on identical briefs, each proving it from the structure constants independently and each refuting the halved form the session first recorded. Derivation and the refutation in [DERIVE-df1.md](../reviews/DERIVE-df1.md).*

**Two consequences the record can use.** The orbit direction $x_i=M_{ki}-M_{ji}$ is an eigenvector with eigenvalue $\theta/2$, all $M$-dependence cancelling against criticality, which is the sharp test the block must pass. And **within-factor pair blocks at $S^p\times S^q$ carry no orbit direction**, since $M_{ji}=M_{ki}$ there, which is why six of the fifteen blocks at $S^3\times S^3$ are orbit-free.

**The within-factor top eigenvalue is $(p-2)/(2(p-1))$, now proved.** [diagonal-half.md](diagonal-half.md) attributed that value to the diagonal restriction while a screen found it in a non-diagonal pair block; **both readings are correct**, it tops each within-factor pair block *and* occurs inside $\mathcal D_n$, and the verification-loop candidate raised against the attribution is closed.

> **A HALVED FORM OF THIS LEMMA WAS RECORDED FIRST AND IS WITHDRAWN.** It read $-\tfrac14M_{jk}$ on the diagonal and $\tfrac14M_{im}$ off it, from applying the polarisation's $-\tfrac14$ to one trace where the two traces are equal. **The test that kills it needs no normalisation**: its row sums vary with $i$, so $\mathbf 1$ is not an eigenvector and it does not preserve the Ricci cut, contradicting [Lemma L120](#lemma-l120-l_m-is-block-diagonal-over-the-sign-characters-at-every-diagonal-critical-point). *It agreed with its own generating code to $9\times10^{-16}$ over $222$ blocks, because those blocks were random diagonal matrices rather than Weyl tensors, and on inputs without Ricci-flatness the failure is invisible.*

#### Lemma L214, the frame split: two exact relations in every frame, and one inequality under the second-order cap

**Fix any orthonormal frame $F$ of $\mathbb R^n$ and split a unit critical point as $W=D+N$, where $D=\pi_FW$ is its coordinate-diagonal part in $F$ and $N=W-D$, with $t=t_F=\lVert D\rVert^2\in[0,1]$ and $a=\langle L_DN,N\rangle$. Then**
$$\theta\,t=P(D)+a,\qquad\theta\,(1-t)=2a+P(N),$$
**and if in addition $L_W\le\tfrac\theta2$ on $W^\perp$, which [Lemma L24](#lemma-l24-second-order-optimality-as-an-eigenvalue-bound-the-canonical-statement) gives at every GLOBAL maximiser, and at a local one with $\theta>0$, then**
$$a\ \ge\ \tfrac\theta2\,t(1-t),\qquad\text{equivalently}\qquad P(D)\ \le\ \tfrac\theta2\,t(1+t).$$

*The two relations and the inequality are proved at $P$'s own operators, through a symmetrised `cubicTri` (`Tsym`), as `L214_op` and `L214_op_max` in [L214Op.lean](../lean/HamiltonsCubic/Results/L214Op.lean), and in every orthonormal frame in [L214Frame.lean](../lean/HamiltonsCubic/Results/L214Frame.lean): `piF`, $\pi_FW=U\pi(U^{\mathsf T}WU)U^{\mathsf T}$, with `piF_diag` and `piF_perp` making it the orthogonal projection onto the operators diagonal in the frame, `L214_frame` for both relations with $\langle D,N\rangle=0$ and $T(D,D,N)=0$, `L214_frame_max` for the inequality at a local maximiser with $\theta>0$, and `L214_frame_global` at every global maximiser at $n\ge4$. [r1786](../reviews/REVIEW_r1786.md) CONFIRMED these. The inequality under the record's own hypothesis, the cap $\langle L_WX,X\rangle\le\tfrac\theta2\lVert X\rVert^2$ on Weyl $X\perp W$ at a unit critical point, is `L214_op_cap`, which is frame-free (any critical Weyl $W$ and any split with $D$ Weyl), and `L214_frame_cap` in every orthonormal frame, with both forms, equivalent by `L214_ineq_iff`, $a\ge\tfrac\theta2t(1-t)$ and $P(D)\le\tfrac\theta2t(1+t)$. $t\in[0,1]$ with $\lVert N\rVert^2=1-t$ is `piF_t_range`, $a=\langle D\circ N,N\rangle$ is `Tsym_eq_fox`, and the uniqueness of $\pi_F$ is `piF_unique`. [r1787](../reviews/REVIEW_r1787.md) CONFIRMED all of it and mapped the lemma's eleven clauses, each covered at the record's hypotheses, so the lemma is typed in full.*

**The whole structural input is [Lemma L120](#lemma-l120-l_m-is-block-diagonal-over-the-sign-characters-at-every-diagonal-critical-point), used at $D$ rather than at a critical point.** *That lemma decomposes $\mathcal W_n=\mathcal D_n\oplus\bigoplus V_{ijkl}\oplus\bigoplus V_{jk}$ into $\Gamma$-isotypic summands and states that $L_M$ preserves each one whenever $M$ is $\Gamma$-fixed, criticality unused. $D$ is $\Gamma$-fixed, since $\mathcal D_n$ is exactly the $\Gamma$-fixed subspace: a sign flip $\varepsilon$ acts on the pair basis by $\varepsilon_i\varepsilon_j$, so an entry off the pair diagonal is fixed only if it vanishes.* **Two consequences, EITHER of which suffices:**
$$D\circ D=L_DD\in\mathcal D_n,\qquad \pi_F(D\circ N)=0,$$
*the first because $L_D$ preserves $\mathcal D_n$ and $D$ lies in it, the second because $N$ lies in the sum of the other summands, which $L_D$ also preserves.* **AND THE TWO SCALARS THEY DELIVER ARE ONE SCALAR**, since $\langle D\circ D,N\rangle=\langle D\circ N,D\rangle$ by the total symmetry of the polarisation: *the proof below needs only $\langle D\circ D,N\rangle=0$, and each consequence of L120 gives it alone.* **Found by typing the lemma**, where the two read as separate hypotheses until the symmetry was applied ([L214FrameSplit.lean](../lean/HamiltonsCubic/Results/L214FrameSplit.lean)'s `hperp`, once).

1. **The two relations.** Expand $Q(W)=W\circ W=D\circ D+2\,D\circ N+N\circ N$ and pair the criticality equation $W\circ W=\theta W$ with $D$ and with $N$ in turn. Pairing with $D$ gives $\theta\langle W,D\rangle=\theta t$ on the right and $P(D)+2\langle D\circ N,D\rangle+\langle N\circ N,D\rangle$ on the left, and $\langle D\circ N,D\rangle=0$ while $\langle N\circ N,D\rangle=\langle D\circ N,N\rangle=a$ by the total symmetry of $\langle X\circ Y,Z\rangle$. Pairing with $N$ gives $\theta\langle W,N\rangle=\theta(1-t)$ against $\langle D\circ D,N\rangle+2a+P(N)$, and the first term is $0$. $\square$
2. **The test vector.** Put $X=tN-(1-t)D$. Then $\langle X,W\rangle=t(1-t)-(1-t)t=0$, so $X\in W^\perp$, and $\lVert X\rVert^2=t^2(1-t)+(1-t)^2t=t(1-t)$.
3. **The cancellation.** $W\circ X=(2t-1)\,D\circ N-(1-t)\,D\circ D+t\,N\circ N$, so pairing with $X$ and killing the two vanishing cross terms leaves $\langle L_WX,X\rangle=a\,(3t^2-2t)+(1-t)^2P(D)+t^2P(N)$. Substituting $P(D)=\theta t-a$ and $P(N)=\theta(1-t)-2a$ from step 1, the coefficient of $a$ is $3t^2-2t-(1-t)^2-2t^2=-1$ identically and the rest is $\theta t(1-t)^2+\theta t^2(1-t)=\theta t(1-t)$, so
$$\langle L_WX,X\rangle=\theta\,t(1-t)-a.$$
4. **The inequality.** [L24](#lemma-l24-second-order-optimality-as-an-eigenvalue-bound-the-canonical-statement) gives $\langle L_WX,X\rangle\le\tfrac\theta2\lVert X\rVert^2=\tfrac\theta2t(1-t)$, whence $a\ge\tfrac\theta2t(1-t)$, and $P(D)=\theta t-a$ turns that into the second form. $\square$

**What is used and what is not.** *Used: criticality in its paired form $\langle Q(W),x\rangle=\theta\langle W,x\rangle$, ONE scalar consequence of [L120](#lemma-l120-l_m-is-block-diagonal-over-the-sign-characters-at-every-diagonal-critical-point) at $D$, the total symmetry of the polarisation, and for the inequality alone [L24](#lemma-l24-second-order-optimality-as-an-eigenvalue-bound-the-canonical-statement) with its $\theta>0$ scope. Not used: the eigenframe, any four-index inequality, any bound on $\Sigma$, diagonality of $W$, or any property of $F$ beyond orthonormality.* **AND THIS BLOCK'S $t$ IS HALF THE RECORD'S.** *Here $t=\lVert D\rVert^2\in[0,1]$ at $\lVert W\rVert=1$, while [conventions.md](conventions.md) fixes $t_F=\lVert K\rVert_F^2$ over ordered pairs with $t_F\le2$, so the two differ by exactly the factor of two that file exists to catch* ([r927](../reviews/REVIEW_r927.md) asked for this to be said and [r928](../reviews/REVIEW_r928.md) found it unsaid). **AND THE TWO RELATIONS ARE ONE**: the second is the first plus the polarisation $P(W)=\theta$ at unit norm, *so a reader checking both is checking one twice* ([r927](../reviews/REVIEW_r927.md)). **THE FRAME IS ARBITRARY AND FIXED THROUGHOUT, AND $t_F$ IS THE FRAME'S OWN VALUE, NEVER $\max_Ft_F$** ([conventions.md](conventions.md) records that substituting the extremal value into a formula meaning $t_F$ is what turned an earlier frame argument false).

**Where it points, and the bound it first suggested is REFUTED.** With $d=D/\sqrt t$ and $\nu=N/\sqrt{1-t}$ unit and $0<t<1$, the first relation reads
$$\langle L_d\nu,\nu\rangle=\frac{\theta\sqrt t-tP(d)}{1-t},\qquad\text{equivalently}\qquad\theta=\sqrt t\,P(d)+\frac{(1-t)}{\sqrt t}\,\langle L_d\nu,\nu\rangle,$$
**and that is an IDENTITY, which is what survives.** *Bounding its two terms by independent suprema, $P(d)\le\Theta_n$ from [L57](problem.md), machine-checked at both parities in [L57Diagonal.lean](../lean/HamiltonsCubic/Results/L57Diagonal.lean) and [L57Odd.lean](../lean/HamiltonsCubic/Results/L57Odd.lean) with the $\lvert Z(v)\rvert^2$ formula derived there, and $\langle L_d\nu,\nu\rangle\le\Lambda_n$, gives $\theta\le\sqrt t\,\Theta_n+(1-t)\Lambda_n/\sqrt t$, and that relaxation is worthless.*

> **THE DECOUPLED BOUND CANNOT BEAT $\Theta_n$ AT ANY $n$** ([r926](../reviews/REVIEW_r926.md) and [r927](../reviews/REVIEW_r927.md) independently, and by screen before them). **THE ANALYTIC REASON BELOW IS [r927](../reviews/REVIEW_r927.md)'S ALONE**, r926 reaching the same ceiling through the padded $S^2\times S^2$ witness instead (*[r928](../reviews/REVIEW_r928.md) found this block citing both for the orbit argument, which r926 does not make*). *At the balanced product the orbit direction $\mathrm{ad}_{E_{kl}}W_0$ is an exact $\tfrac12$-eigenvector of $L_e$ by [L24](#lemma-l24-second-order-optimality-as-an-eigenvalue-bound-the-canonical-statement)'s sharpness clause, and it lies in $\mathcal D_n^\perp$ because $E_{kl}$ carries a nontrivial sign character, so $\Lambda_n\ge\Theta_n/2$ with no witness needed. Then $f(t)=\sqrt t\,\Theta_n+(1-t)\Lambda_n/\sqrt t$ has $f'\le0$ on $(0,1]$ and $\inf f=f(1)=\Theta_n$, by $\tfrac{\Theta_n}2(u+u^{-1})\ge\Theta_n$ at $u=\sqrt t$.* **And $\Lambda_n\ge\sqrt3/2=0.866$ in fact**, from a padded $S^2\times S^2$ through [L121](#lemma-l121-the-4-set-block-its-spectrum-and-one-necessary-condition-per-4-subset), where reaching $\sqrt{3/2}$ at $n=12$ would need $\Lambda_{12}\le0.5347$. *At the conjectured extremal non-diagonal point the bound gives $1.4864$, slack by $21\%$, because $\Lambda_n$ discards criticality: its maximiser violates L121's own $\Sigma\le\theta^2$ by $3$ against $0.75$.*
>
> **So the lossy step is the DECOUPLING, and it is named as [post F67](INDEX-traps.md): $P(d)$ and $\langle L_d\nu,\nu\rangle$ are one number at the configurations that matter**, $\sqrt3/2$ at padded $\mathbb{CP}^2$, and any successor must keep the pair coupled through the identity rather than bound them separately. *This is the fifth route in five days to close by a relaxation being tight at the balanced product.*

**Prior art, which the first version of this block did not credit** ([r926](../reviews/REVIEW_r926.md), [r927](../reviews/REVIEW_r927.md)). *The frame split itself and its norm bookkeeping appear in [DERIVE-mx1](../reviews/DERIVE-mx1.md), [bispectral.md](bispectral.md) and [target.md](target.md); what is new here is the pair of exact relations, the cancellation, and the inequality.* **And $D\in\mathcal W_n$ needs no argument beyond [L123](#lemma-l123-a-zero-diagonal-operator-contributes-nothing-to-a-single-character-rayleigh-quotient)'s**, Ricci-flatness being a condition on frame-diagonal entries alone.

**Gate 1, in the record's convention** ([screen_frame_split.py](../scripts/runs/screen_frame_split.py)). *Checked at $S^4\times S^4$, whose $\theta=1.145643924$ and criticality residual $3\times10^{-16}$ fix the convention, in sixty random frames with $t_F\in[0.018,0.192]$: both relations hold to $3\times10^{-15}$, and neither inequality is violated.* *Typed in [L214FrameSplit.lean](../lean/HamiltonsCubic/Results/L214FrameSplit.lean), eight declarations `sorry`-free on the three standard axioms, over an abstract totally symmetric trilinear form: the two relations, the cancellation by an exact `linear_combination` certificate, the test vector's orthogonality and norm, and the inequality. **The frame appears there only as the scalar `hperp`**, so a reader can see that nothing else about $F$ is used.* **Gate 2 LANDED, CONFIRMED by both readers** ([r926](../reviews/REVIEW_r926.md), [r927](../reviews/REVIEW_r927.md)), *each finding no use of the eigenframe, no confusion of $t_F$ with $\max_Ft_F$, and the cancellation exact, and each refuting the decoupled bound above.*

#### Lemma L284, the mixing-gap variance identity: at a critical point split by sign characters, the frame-split defect is three orthogonal defects

*Gate 2 PASSED ([r1793](../reviews/REVIEW_r1793.md), [r1794](../reviews/REVIEW_r1794.md), independently, every claim CONFIRMED). **The identity is the outside author's**, Part II's exact variance formula (86) in an outside report archive (unpublished, held in the private record), checked here on the record's objects for [I121](../plan.md), route [R52](../routes.md) ([note](../reviews/2026-09-28-I121-MG-audit.md)). Typed and proved in [MixingGapVariance.lean](../lean/HamiltonsCubic/Results/MixingGapVariance.lean) as `identity_86`, over any real inner product space with a symmetric product whose multiplications are self-adjoint; the six character hypotheses are L120's block diagonality.*

**Statement.** *Let $W\in\mathcal W_n$ be a unit critical point, $Q(W)=\rho W$ with $\rho\ne0$, and fix any orthonormal frame. Write $W=A+B$ with $A=\pi_{\mathcal D}W$, $t=\lVert A\rVert$, $b=\lVert B\rVert$, $0<t<1$, and $B=\sum_UB_U$ over the nontrivial sign characters the frame induces, $p_U=\lVert B_U\rVert^2$. Put $q_\perp=Q(A)-\tfrac{P(A)}{t^2}A$, $Q(A,B_U)=\lambda_UB_U+Y_U$ with $Y_U\perp B_U$ (for $B_U\ne0$), $\bar\lambda=\sum_Up_U\lambda_U/b^2$, and*
$$z=\frac{A/t-tW}b,\qquad\mathcal U=\tfrac12I+\tfrac12W\otimes W-\rho^{-1}L_W,\qquad\eta=\langle z,\mathcal Uz\rangle,\qquad C=(\mathcal U-\eta)z .$$
*Then*
$$\rho^2t^2b^2\lVert C\rVert^2=\lVert q_\perp\rVert^2+\sum_U\lVert Y_U\rVert^2+\sum_Up_U(\lambda_U-\bar\lambda)^2,\qquad\bar\lambda=\rho t^2\big(\eta+\tfrac12\big).$$
*Neither the choice of frame nor global maximality enters.*

**Proof.** (1) $A/t-tW=\tfrac{b^2}tA-tB$, using $A\perp B$ (distinct characters), so $\lVert z\rVert=1$ and $z\perp W$. (2) $\mathcal UW=0$ by criticality and $\lVert W\rVert=1$; $\mathcal U$ is self-adjoint because $L_W$ is, so $\langle C,W\rangle=\langle z,\mathcal UW\rangle=0$, and $\langle C,z\rangle=0$ is the definition of $\eta$; as $t,b\ne0$, $\mathop{\mathrm{span}}\{W,z\}=\mathop{\mathrm{span}}\{A,B\}$, so $C\perp A$ and $C\perp B$. (3) The trivial character is exactly the diagonal operators (the $(ij,kl)$ entry carries the character of $\{i,j\}\triangle\{k,l\}$), and $Q$ is equivariant, so $Q(A)$ is diagonal and $Q(A,B_U)$ lies in character $U$; projecting $C=(\tfrac12-\eta)z-\rho^{-1}Q(W,z)$ gives $\pi_{\mathcal D}C=\big((\tfrac12-\eta)\tfrac bt+\tfrac tb\big)A-\tfrac{Q(A)}{\rho tb}$ and $\pi_UC=\tfrac{t(\frac12+\eta)}bB_U-\tfrac{\lambda_UB_U+Y_U}{\rho tb}$. (4) $\langle C,A\rangle=0$ forces $(\tfrac12-\eta)b^2+t^2=P(A)/(\rho t^2)$, so $\pi_{\mathcal D}C=-q_\perp/(\rho tb)$; $\langle C,B\rangle=0$ forces $\bar\lambda=\rho t^2(\eta+\tfrac12)$, so $\pi_UC=-\big(Y_U+(\lambda_U-\bar\lambda)B_U\big)/(\rho tb)$, characters with $B_U=0$ carrying no weight. (5) Pythagoras over the orthogonal characters, with $Y_U\perp B_U$.

**Against the record.** *Agreement on the scalar part, with [L214](#lemma-l214-the-frame-split-two-exact-relations-in-every-frame-and-one-inequality-under-the-second-order-cap).* Up to sign and scale $z$ is L214's test vector $X=TN-(1-T)D$ with $T=t^2=\lVert D\rVert^2$, $D=A$, $N=B$ ([r1794](../reviews/REVIEW_r1794.md)), so for $\rho>0$, $\eta\ge0$ is exactly L214's inequality $P(A)\le\tfrac\rho2T(1+T)$, and step (4)'s relation is L214's first relation through $a=b^2\bar\lambda=\rho T(1-T)(\eta+\tfrac12)$. **New to the record: the split of $\lVert C\rVert^2$ by sign character into three independent nonnegative defects.** *So a bound on $\lVert q_\perp\rVert$ alone cannot control $C$, and a proof of the mixing gap through it must use all three, which is [F67](fence-posts.md)'s coupling in another form.*

#### Lemma L215, an explicit bound on the quadratic map

**For every $X\in\mathcal W_n$,**
$$\lVert X^{\#}\rVert\le(n-2)\lVert X\rVert^2,\qquad\text{hence}\qquad\lVert Q(X)\rVert\le(n-1)\lVert X\rVert^2.$$
**This is the UPPER bound on $\kappa$ that [I96](../plan.md) owed** and that no screen could give, a sampled supremum bounding $\kappa$ from below ([r928](../reviews/REVIEW_r928.md)). **Gate 2 landed, the bound CONFIRMED by both readers with every step valid and every direction conservative** ([r929](../reviews/REVIEW_r929.md), [r930](../reviews/REVIEW_r930.md)).

**THE ENGINE IS [LEMMA L153](bounds.md#lemma-l153--the-killing-row-marginal-sum_betalvertv_alphav_betarvert22n-2) POLARISED, WHICH THIS FILE ALREADY STATED 355 LINES ABOVE.** *The invariant needed is $\sum_aA_a^{\mathsf T}A_a=2(n-2)\,\mathrm{Id}$, equivalently $\sum_{\alpha\gamma}c_{\alpha\beta\gamma}c_{\alpha\beta'\gamma}=2(n-2)\delta_{\beta\beta'}$, and that stands verbatim in this file's own "what the bounds use" paragraph beside $\lVert c\rVert^2=2(n-2)N$.* **L153 proves it for any $v$ at every $n\ge2$ and passed Gate 2** ([r695](../reviews/REVIEW_r695.md), [r696](../reviews/REVIEW_r696.md)), *so the first version of this block, which deduced the identity from Schur's lemma plus the count $\sum c_{amp}^2=n(n-1)(n-2)$, was both a sixth restatement and the weaker route: Schur owes a clause at $n=4$, where $\Lambda^2$ splits, and L153 needs no irreducibility at all* ([r929](../reviews/REVIEW_r929.md), [r930](../reviews/REVIEW_r930.md), independently). **The Schur argument is withdrawn and the citation replaces it.**

1. **The square.** $\lVert X^2\rVert^2=\sum_i\lambda_i^4\le(\sum_i\lambda_i^2)^2=\lVert X\rVert^4$, using that $X$ is symmetric, so $\lVert X^2\rVert\le\lVert X\rVert^2$ with equality exactly at rank one.
2. **The sharp part.** $\lvert\mathop{\mathrm{tr}}(A_aXA_bX)\rvert\le\lVert A_aX\rVert\lVert A_bX\rVert$ by Cauchy--Schwarz on the trace pairing, and then
$$\lVert X^{\#}\rVert^2=\sum_{ab}\tfrac14\mathop{\mathrm{tr}}(A_aXA_bX)^2\le\tfrac14\Big(\sum_a\lVert A_aX\rVert^2\Big)^2=\tfrac14\big(2(n-2)\lVert X\rVert^2\big)^2,$$
*the last step an IDENTITY rather than a second inequality, $\sum_{ab}u_au_b=(\sum_au_a)^2$, and the value from L153's invariant.* Hence $\lVert X^{\#}\rVert\le(n-2)\lVert X\rVert^2$.
3. **Together** $\lVert Q(X)\rVert\le\lVert X^2\rVert+\lVert X^{\#}\rVert\le(n-1)\lVert X\rVert^2$. $\square$

**The proof uses no property of $\mathcal W_n$ beyond symmetry**, so the bound holds on all of $\mathrm{Sym}^2(\Lambda^2)$ and the lemma is stronger than stated ([r929](../reviews/REVIEW_r929.md)).

> **A SHARPER CONSTANT IS AVAILABLE FROM THE SAME INGREDIENTS AND ITS VALUE IS NOT YET SETTLED.** *Mixing the operator and Frobenius norms in step 2, rather than using Cauchy--Schwarz twice, gives $\lVert X^{\#}\rVert\le\sqrt{c(n-2)}\lVert X\rVert^2$ and takes the radius from $n^{-2}$ to $n^{-3/2}$.* **Three readings of $c$ are in play and they differ by factors of two**: $\sqrt{2(n-2)}$ ([r930](../reviews/REVIEW_r930.md)), $\sqrt{n-2}$ ([r929](../reviews/REVIEW_r929.md), from $\lVert A_a\rVert_{\mathrm{op}}=\sqrt2$), and $\sqrt{(n-2)/2}$ from $\lVert A_a\rVert_{\mathrm{op}}=1$, which is what the record's own $c$ measures at every $n$ tested alongside $\lVert A_a\rVert_F^2=2(n-2)$. *[conventions.md](conventions.md)'s rule applies: settle it against a derived identity rather than a numeral, and until it is settled the weakest reading is the one this block claims.*
>
> **AND THE SHARPEST READING IS A LEMMA THE RECORD ALREADY CARRIES.** *[Lemma L143](bounds.md#lemma-l143-suplvert-prvertle1sqrtn-2-from-the-two-recorded-marginals) is $\sup\lvert P\rvert\le1+\sqrt{n-2}$, derived from exactly these two marginals, Gate 2 passed, and [post F10](fence-posts.md)'s marginal LP beats it, $1.606$ against $3.000$ at $n=6$ and $2.53$ against $4.162$ at $n=12$.* **So what this block contributes is the statement about the OPERATOR norm $\lVert Q(X)\rVert$ rather than the scalar $\lvert P(X)\rvert$**, which is what the isolation estimate consumes, *and even there the constant $n-1$ is weaker than the $1+\sqrt{n-2}$ the same ingredients give once the normalisation is settled.* **This is the fourth place in one cycle where the record already held what was being derived**, after L153, this file's own invariants paragraph, and the typed contraction.

> **AND THE TWO EQUALITY CASES EXCLUDE EACH OTHER, SO THE SUM IS NEVER TIGHT** ([r930](../reviews/REVIEW_r930.md)). *Step 1 is tight exactly at rank one, and a rank-one $X$ has $X^{\#}=0$.* **The measured slack is a factor of $7.9$ at $n=6$ and $8.1$ at $n=8$**, so the block's earlier claim of more than an order of magnitude was wrong at both, and the sampled maxima it quoted, $0.504$ and $0.352$, are below what a longer search finds, $0.611$ and $0.416$.

**Gate 1.** *L153's invariant checked at $n=3$ to $12$ with zero residual, including both $\Lambda^2_\pm$ blocks at $n=4$; $\mathrm{id}^{\#}=(n-2)\mathrm{id}$ exact; and the bound checked against sampled maxima at $n=5,6,8,10,12$, conservative everywhere.* **The ordered reading of the invariant is the correct one**, the unordered one would have claimed a bound twice as strong as is derivable, and no screen here could have caught that ([r930](../reviews/REVIEW_r930.md)).

#### From [I96](../plan.md): the isolation radius is NOT established, and what the two readings of that leave

**The radius $2\theta_0(n)/((n-2)(n-1))$ that [L215](#lemma-l215-an-explicit-bound-on-the-quadratic-map)'s first version claimed is withdrawn.** *Gate 2 refused it twice over, and the two refusals are different and both right.*

> **REFUTED IN A FIXED FRAME, WITH A WITNESS** ([r929](../reviews/REVIEW_r929.md), reproduced here independently). *Rotating the balanced product along a gauge direction, $W_\varepsilon=\Lambda^2(\exp\varepsilon(E_{04}-E_{40}))\cdot W_0$, gives genuinely critical points, residual $3\times10^{-16}$ and $\theta$ unchanged to nine digits, at $\lVert N\rVert=0.0264$ and $0.0132$ at $n=8$, below the claimed floor $0.0546$.* **The gauge orbit is a kernel direction of $2L_D-\theta$ by construction, so a vanishing excess cannot rescue this**: the dropped hypothesis is the transversality of $N$, which the Lean file carries as a binder and the prose statement did not.

> **AND VACUOUS WHERE IT WAS STATED, WHICH IS THE SHARPER OBJECTION** ([r930](../reviews/REVIEW_r930.md)). *The only $\gamma$ in hand belongs to $D$ equal to the UNIT balanced product, and there $\lVert D\rVert=1$ forces $N=0$ at unit $\lVert W\rVert$. Any $N\ne0$ makes $\lVert D\rVert<1$, off the one configuration whose gap is known.* **So the informative branch of the dichotomy was empty at the only place the constants applied**, and the perturbation step is not a refinement but the whole content.

**THE GAUGE HALF HAS A REPAIR, DERIVED AND VERIFIED HERE.** *Take $F$ to maximise $t_F$. Varying the frame along $\xi\in\mathfrak{so}(n)$ gives $t'(0)=-2\langle\mathrm{ad}_\xi W,D\rangle$, so at a maximum $D\perp T_W\mathcal O$, and since $\langle W,\mathrm{ad}_\xi W\rangle=0$ always, $N\perp T_W\mathcal O$ too.* **Transversality is therefore free at the maximising frame**, and the witness above is no counterexample there: rotated back to its own maximising frame it has $t=1.000000$, stationary to $5\times10^{-18}$, and $\lVert N\rVert=10^{-8}$, which is the dichotomy's first branch. *So the statement to aim at reads: at the frame maximising $t_F$, either $W$ is coordinate-diagonal in some frame or $\lVert N\rVert\ge\gamma/\kappa$, and "coordinate-diagonal in no frame" is exactly the open problem's hypothesis.*

**What [I96](../plan.md) owes is therefore unchanged in size and clearer in kind**: the gap $\gamma$ for a $D$ that is NOT the unit balanced product, which is the whole of the radius, and the vanishing excess as an explicit hypothesis, failing at $n=6$ where the kernel is $18$-dimensional against a $9$-dimensional orbit. *The constants themselves are settled: $\kappa\le n-1$ by [L215](#lemma-l215-an-explicit-bound-on-the-quadratic-map), and the gauge fixing by the paragraph above.*

#### Lemma L216, an explicit isolation radius at every quantitatively nondegenerate critical orbit

**Let $W_0$ be a unit critical point of $P$ with $\theta_0=P(W_0)>0$ and $e=W_0/\theta_0$, and suppose that $\langle L_eH,H\rangle\le\tfrac12-\gamma$ for every unit $H\perp W_0$ orthogonal to the orbit tangent $T_{W_0}\mathcal O$, with $\gamma>0$. Then no critical point of $P$ on the unit sphere of $\mathcal W_n$ lies within geodesic distance**
$$r=\frac{4\,\theta_0\,\gamma}{9\,\big(1+\sqrt{n-2}\big)}$$
**of the orbit $\mathcal O=O(n)\cdot W_0$, other than the points of $\mathcal O$ itself.**

**Corollary, at the sphere products.** *For $S^p\times S^q$ with $p,q\ge3$ and $(p,q)\ne(3,3)$, [L27](nondegeneracy.md) makes the $\tfrac12$-eigenspace exactly the orbit tangent and, with [L26](nondegeneracy.md)'s $\mu=\frac{n-2}{2(p-1)(q-1)}$, gives the gap in closed form,* $$\gamma_{p,q}=\min\Big(\frac1{2(p-1)},\ \frac1{2(q-1)},\ \frac12-\mu\Big).$$ **At the balanced product, $n=2m\ge8$, this is $\gamma=1/(n-2)$ and**
$$r_n=\frac{4\,\theta_0(n)}{9\,(n-2)\,\big(1+\sqrt{n-2}\big)}=\Theta\big(n^{-3/2}\big),$$
*which is $0.0246$ at $n=8$ and $0.0132$ at $n=12$.*

*Lean: the radius's arithmetic is [L216IsolationRadius.lean](../lean/HamiltonsCubic/Results/L216IsolationRadius.lean), and its Bernstein hypothesis is discharged in [L216Bern.lean](../lean/HamiltonsCubic/Results/L216Bern.lean): `riesz` (M. Riesz's interpolation formula for binary cubic forms) gives `bern3`, $\lvert\varphi'''\rvert\le27\sup\lvert\varphi\rvert$ on the unit circle; `P_plane` writes $P(xW+yH)$ as a binary cubic; and `no_critical_in_tube_P` is step 5 along $\sigma\mapsto P(\cos\sigma\,W+\sin\sigma\,H)$, with the gap hypothesis in the record's form as `no_critical_in_tube_Le` in [L217Le.lean](../lean/HamiltonsCubic/Results/L217Le.lean), asking $\lvert P\rvert\le M$ only on the unit circle of $\mathop{\mathrm{span}}(W,H)$, which lies in the unit Weyl sphere where [L143](bounds.md)'s $M$ applies ([r1761](../reviews/REVIEW_r1761.md)). At the balanced sphere product the gap is typed and consumed in [L216Sphere.lean](../lean/HamiltonsCubic/Results/L216Sphere.lean): `Le_gap_balanced` gives $\gamma=\frac1{2(m-1)}$ at $p=q=m\ge4$ from L3's Hessian bound, step 1 and the final inference are typed at every gapped critical orbit by `L216_general` in [L216Gen.lean](../lean/HamiltonsCubic/Results/L216Gen.lean), with $M$ a bound on $\lvert P\rvert$ over the unit Weyl sphere, the record's radius is `L216_radius` at L143's $M=1+\sqrt{n-2}$ and the corollary is `L216_sphere_radius` ([r1906](../reviews/REVIEW_r1906.md)), and `L216_sphere_deriv` states step 5 there as a negative derivative of $\sigma\mapsto P(\cos\sigma\,M+\sin\sigma\,H)$ ([r1866](../reviews/REVIEW_r1866.md)); at every sphere product with $p,q\ge3$ off $(3,3)$, [L216Unbal.lean](../lean/HamiltonsCubic/Results/L216Unbal.lean)'s `Le_gap_sphere` gives $\gamma_{p,q}=\tfrac12-\max(\lambda_p,\lambda_q,\mu)$, which is this block's $\min\big(\frac1{2(p-1)},\frac1{2(q-1)},\tfrac12-\mu\big)$, `L216_sphere_general` step 5 with it, and `horb_of_orbit` the passage from the orbit tangent to the $\tfrac12$-eigenvectors ([r1867](../reviews/REVIEW_r1867.md)).*

**This is the first explicit isolation radius on the record.** *[L19](problem.md)'s block records that "no isolation radius $r_i$ is anywhere on the record" and [I35](../plan.md) was filed to supply one, its own note saying the radius needs a second-variation bound AND a third-order remainder, the latter "a computation at the balanced orbit and not transferable".* **For $P$ itself the remainder IS transferable**, being controlled by [L143](bounds.md)'s GLOBAL bound on $\lvert P\rvert$, so the only orbit-specific input is the gap. *And it supersedes the isolation estimate of the block above, whose radius was refuted in a fixed frame and vacuous where stated: here every constant is evaluated on the orbit and carried along it by equivariance, never at a perturbed diagonal part, and the gauge is handled by the nearest-point geodesic rather than by a hypothesis.*

1. **The geodesic.** $\mathcal O$ is compact, so for $W$ on the sphere at distance $s=\mathop{\mathrm{dist}}(W,\mathcal O)$ with $0<s<r$ the distance is attained at some $W_1=g\cdot W_0$, and by the first variation the minimising great circle leaves $W_1$ with unit velocity $H\perp T_{W_1}\mathcal O$, also $H\perp W_1$. So $W=\cos s\,W_1+\sin s\,H$, exact for $0<s<\pi$. *By equivariance, $P(g\cdot X)=P(X)$ and $L_{e_1}=gL_eg^{-1}$ with $e_1=W_1/\theta_0$, so the gap hypothesis holds at $W_1$ with the same $\gamma$ and $\theta_0$. The nearest point need not be unique, any minimiser serving.* **And $r\le\tfrac23$, so every $s$ the proof meets lies well inside $(0,\pi)$**: Bernstein twice gives $\lvert\phi''(0)\rvert\le9M$, and step 3 gives $6\theta_0\gamma\le\lvert\phi''(0)\rvert$, so $r=4\theta_0\gamma/(9M)\le\tfrac23$ ([r932](../reviews/REVIEW_r932.md)). *The first version wrote $s\in(0,\pi/2)$ and never showed $r$ below it, which [r931](../reviews/REVIEW_r931.md) found, repairing it by $r\le\pi$ since $-W_1$ is critical on every normal circle.* **The nearest-point argument is [L134](route-a.md)(d)'s, which already joins a point near an orbit to its nearest orbit point by a normal geodesic.**
2. **The function along it.** Put $\phi(\sigma)=P(\cos\sigma\,W_1+\sin\sigma\,H)$. It is a homogeneous cubic in $(\cos\sigma,\sin\sigma)$, hence a trigonometric polynomial of degree at most $3$, and every point of the circle is a unit vector of $\mathcal W_n$, so [L143](bounds.md) gives $\lvert\phi\rvert\le M:=1+\sqrt{n-2}$ everywhere.
3. **Its first two derivatives at the orbit.** $\phi'(0)=DP_{W_1}[H]=3\langle Q(W_1),H\rangle=3\theta_0\langle W_1,H\rangle=0$. And writing the great circle as $c(\sigma)=\cos\sigma\,W_1+\sin\sigma\,H$, so that $c''=-c$, $\phi''(0)=D^2P_{W_1}[H,H]+DP_{W_1}[c''(0)]=D^2P_{W_1}[H,H]-DP_{W_1}[W_1]=6\theta_0\langle L_{e_1}H,H\rangle-3\theta_0=6\theta_0\big(\langle L_{e_1}H,H\rangle-\tfrac12\big)\le-6\theta_0\gamma$, which is [L24](#lemma-l24-second-order-optimality-as-an-eigenvalue-bound-the-canonical-statement)'s Hessian read along one direction.
4. **The third derivative, by Bernstein.** For a trigonometric polynomial $T$ of degree $N$, $\lVert T'\rVert_\infty\le N\lVert T\rVert_\infty$ ([Bernstein's inequality](../references/queffelec-zarouf-2019-bernstein-inequality/notes.md)), and $T'$ is again of degree $N$, so iterating three times gives $\lVert\phi'''\rVert_\infty\le27M$.
5. **The sign.** Taylor's theorem with the two bounds gives $\phi'(\sigma)\le-6\theta_0\gamma\,\sigma+\tfrac{27M}2\sigma^2$, which is negative for $0<\sigma<\frac{12\theta_0\gamma}{27M}=r$. *If $W=\phi$'s point at $\sigma=s$ were critical, the Riemannian gradient of $P$ there would vanish, and $\phi'(s)$ is that gradient paired with the unit tangent of the circle, so $\phi'(s)=0$.* **That contradicts the negativity just shown whenever $0<s<r$.** $\square$

**What is used**: criticality, [L24](#lemma-l24-second-order-optimality-as-an-eigenvalue-bound-the-canonical-statement)'s Hessian formula, [L143](bounds.md), Bernstein's classical inequality, $O(n)$-equivariance, and the first variation of distance. *For the corollary, [L27](nondegeneracy.md) and [L26](nondegeneracy.md). Not used: [L145](#lemma-l145-the-constant-chain-for-the-balanced-orbit-tube-radius-and-the-lower-bound-r_nomegan-32-it-supports)'s penalised functional or the defect $\delta$, any frame, [L214](#lemma-l214-the-frame-split-two-exact-relations-in-every-frame-and-one-inequality-under-the-second-order-cap), or the excess measurement, which L27 proves.*

> **WHAT IT DOES NOT GIVE, STATED BEFORE ANYONE READS MORE INTO IT.** *It does not close the conjecture: the localisation it would finish, that every critical point above the rival value lies within $\varepsilon_n$ of a rigid orbit, has no proposition on the record, [L19](problem.md) having been withdrawn as carrying none.* **So it closes the RADIUS half of that route and nothing else.** *Padded $\mathbb{CP}^2$ is one input away: its normal gap is $\gamma=\tfrac16$, measured exactly at $n=8,10$ with excess zero, and it is [L21](route-a.md)'s $\alpha\le\tfrac13$ read through the Hessian, which gives $r=2\sqrt{3/2}/(27(1+\sqrt{n-2}))\approx0.0218$ at $n=12$.* **That corollary is CONDITIONAL on L21's one unproved input, that its four blocks $F_\lambda$ are $m$-free**, which is [I54](../plan-verification.md) in the verification queue. *The lemma applies at saddles with a positive gap too, maximality never being used* ([r931](../reviews/REVIEW_r931.md)).

> **THE MARGIN STEP IS EXACT AND THE LOSS IS ALL IN THE REMAINDER.** *Screened over $440$ normal directions: $\sup_H\phi''(0)$ equals $-6\theta_0\gamma$ to every printed digit at the balanced product at $n=8$ and $n=12$ and at $S^3\times S^5$, where the measured $\gamma=0.125$ is the corollary's formula exactly. Bernstein's $27M$ exceeds the sampled $\sup\lvert\phi'''\rvert$ by a factor of about ten, and L143 is itself beaten by [post F10](fence-posts.md)'s LP, so the radius is a floor. No sampled direction turns $\phi'$ nonnegative anywhere below $\sigma=0.6$.* **$\Theta(n^{-3/2})$ is true of the formula for $r_n$, and the true isolation radius is only $\Omega(n^{-3/2})$**, $r_n$ being a sufficient radius ([r932](../reviews/REVIEW_r932.md)), *which is exactly the misreading that refuted [L145](#lemma-l145-the-constant-chain-for-the-balanced-orbit-tube-radius-and-the-lower-bound-r_nomegan-32-it-supports)'s $\Theta$.* **The exponent agrees with L145's because the mechanism is shared, not because the radii are compared** ([r931](../reviews/REVIEW_r931.md)): *[L147](#lemma-l147-the-tube-radius-tracks-suplvert-prvert-on-betain0tfrac12-and-the-route-ends-at-thetan-1)'s $P$-side branch already reads a margin $3\theta_0/(n-2)$ over $\sup\lvert P\rvert$ along normal geodesics, for the penalised functional, and both reach $n^{-3/2}$ through $M=O(\sqrt n)$ against a gap $\Theta(1/n)$* ([r932](../reviews/REVIEW_r932.md)). **What is new here is the conclusion for $\mathrm{Crit}(P)$ and the transfer of the remainder to every orbit**, both reviewers finding no explicit radius for $P$ itself anywhere on the record.

**Gate 1** ([screen_isolation_radius.py](../scripts/runs/screen_isolation_radius.py)). *The balanced product at $n=8$ and $n=12$ and $S^3\times S^5$ at $n=8$: criticality residual below $3\times10^{-15}$, the $\tfrac12$-eigenspace equal to the orbit dimension $pq$, the gap equal to the corollary's closed form, the margin attained, and $\phi'<0$ on the whole sampled range.* **Gate 2 LANDED, the lemma and its corollary CONFIRMED by both readers** ([r931](../reviews/REVIEW_r931.md), [r932](../reviews/REVIEW_r932.md), identical briefs, independently), *each re-deriving every step by hand and each screening the gap formula at splits Gate 1 never used, $(3,4)$, $(4,5)$, $(5,5)$ and $(3,6)$, to twelve digits, $(3,4)$ being the one where $\mu$ binds at $\tfrac1{12}$. Their only disagreement was step 1's range, a wording repair both resolved and applied above.*

#### Lemma L217, the value bootstrap at a gapped critical orbit: a global maximiser within $\arcsin\min(3\gamma,\tfrac12)$ makes the orbit itself maximal

*Gate 2 PASSED, [r933](../reviews/REVIEW_r933.md) and [r934](../reviews/REVIEW_r934.md) on identical briefs, the lemma and corollaries (a) and (b) CONFIRMED by both. Derived 2026-09-14 as [I98](../plan.md) on route [R25](../routes.md). The arithmetic is kernel-checked in [L217.lean](../lean/HamiltonsCubic/Results/L217.lean), and the chain is instantiated at $P$'s own operators, through a symmetrised `cubicTri` (`Tsym`), as `L217_op` and `L217_op_eq` in [L217Op.lean](../lean/HamiltonsCubic/Results/L217Op.lean), with the gap hypothesis in the record's form $\langle L_eH,H\rangle\le\tfrac12-\gamma$ as `L217_op_Le` in [L217Le.lean](../lean/HamiltonsCubic/Results/L217Le.lean) through `Tsym_eq_Le` ([r1775](../reviews/REVIEW_r1775.md), CONFIRMED WITH OBSERVATIONS). Criticality and the gap are carried from $u$ to every orbit point $u'=UuU^{\mathsf T}$ by `crit_rotR`, `normal_rotR` and `normal_rotR_surj`, and `L217_orbit` gives $P(W^*)=\theta$ at $u'$ from the hypotheses at $u$ ([L217Orbit.lean](../lean/HamiltonsCubic/Results/L217Orbit.lean), [r1789](../reviews/REVIEW_r1789.md)). Corollaries (a) and (b) are kernel-checked in full as `L217_balanced`, `L217_sphere` and `L217_no_max_near` in [L217Bal.lean](../lean/HamiltonsCubic/Results/L217Bal.lean), the geodesic and the first-variation step supplied by the maximiser of the orbit pairing in [L3Local.lean](../lean/HamiltonsCubic/Results/L3Local.lean); and the lemma itself at every gapped critical orbit is `L217_general` in [L217Gen.lean](../lean/HamiltonsCubic/Results/L217Gen.lean), the first-variation step from the derivative of the orbit action along each coordinate rotation ([RotDeriv.lean](../lean/HamiltonsCubic/Results/RotDeriv.lean)).*

**Statement.** *Let $u$ be a unit critical point of $P$ on the unit sphere of $\mathcal W_n$ with $\theta_u=P(u)>0$ and $e=u/\theta_u$, and suppose $\langle L_eH,H\rangle\le\tfrac12-\gamma$ for every unit $H\perp u$ orthogonal to the orbit tangent $T_u\mathcal O$, $\mathcal O=O(n)\cdot u$, with $\gamma\ge0$. Let $W^\ast$ be a global maximiser of $P$ on the unit sphere, $m=P(W^\ast)$, at geodesic distance $\varphi\in[0,\tfrac\pi2]$ from $\mathcal O$ with $\sin\varphi\le\min(3\gamma,\tfrac12)$. Then $m\le\theta_u$, and since $m\ge P(u)=\theta_u$ by the definition of $m$, $m=\theta_u$: the orbit $\mathcal O$ is itself a set of global maximisers.*

**Corollaries.**

- **(a) The balanced orbit.** *At $n=2k\ge12$ and $u$ the unit Weyl tensor of $S^k\times S^k$, [L216](#lemma-l216-an-explicit-isolation-radius-at-every-quantitatively-nondegenerate-critical-orbit)'s corollary gives $\gamma=1/(n-2)$.* **So if some global maximiser lies within geodesic angle $\arcsin\frac3{n-2}$ of the balanced orbit, then $\sup P=\theta_0(n)$**, which is Conjecture A's value. *The angle is $0.3047$ at $n=12$, against L216's critical-point radius $0.0132$.*
- **(b) Every sub-maximal sphere product with $p,q\ge3$ and $(p,q)\ne(3,3)$.** *For such $S^p\times S^q$ with $\theta_{p,q}<\Theta_n$, with L216's gap $\gamma_{p,q}$, no global maximiser lies within geodesic angle $\arcsin\min(3\gamma_{p,q},\tfrac12)$ of its orbit*, since $m=\theta_{p,q}<\Theta_n\le m$ would follow. *More generally, no global maximiser lies in such a tube about any gapped critical orbit whose value is below $\sup P$.*
- **(c) Padded $\mathbb{CP}^2$, for comparison only.** *At $n\ge12$, where $\sqrt{3/2}<\Theta_n$, and conditional on [L21](route-a.md)'s $\alpha\le\tfrac13$, whose $m$-freeness of four $F_\lambda$ blocks is owed as [I54](../plan-verification.md), its gap is $\gamma=\tfrac16$, so (b)'s device puts every global maximiser at $\varphi>\tfrac\pi6$ from the orbit, weaker than [L20](route-a.md)'s $0.5748$, which solves the envelope exactly rather than bounding it.*
