**Proof.** (1) *The geodesic.* $\mathcal O$ is compact, so $\varphi$ is attained at some $u_1=g\cdot u$, and for $\varphi>0$ the minimising great circle leaves $u_1$ with a unit velocity $V\perp u_1$ and $V\perp T_{u_1}\mathcal O$ by the first variation of distance, so $W^\ast=c\,u_1+s\,V$ with $c=\cos\varphi\ge0$, $s=\sin\varphi$. By equivariance $P(u_1)=\theta_u$ and the gap holds at $u_1$ with $e_1=u_1/\theta_u$. At $\varphi=0$, $W^\ast\in\mathcal O$ and $m=\theta_u$.

(2) *The exact expansion.* With $T(X,Y,Z)=\langle X\circ Y,Z\rangle$ the fully symmetric polarisation of $P$ ([Lemma L24](#lemma-l24-second-order-optimality-as-an-eigenvalue-bound-the-canonical-statement)'s argument), $P(c\,u_1+s\,V)=c^3P(u_1)+3c^2s\,T(u_1,u_1,V)+3cs^2\,T(u_1,V,V)+s^3P(V)$. Criticality gives $T(u_1,u_1,V)=\langle Q(u_1),V\rangle=\theta_u\langle u_1,V\rangle=0$, and $T(u_1,V,V)=\langle L_{u_1}V,V\rangle=\theta_u\langle L_{e_1}V,V\rangle$. So
$$m=\theta_u\big(c^3+3cs^2\alpha\big)+s^3P(V),\qquad\alpha=\langle L_{e_1}V,V\rangle\le\tfrac12-\gamma.$$

(3) *The global comparison.* $V$ is a unit element of $\mathcal W_n$ and $W^\ast$ is a global maximiser, so $P(V)\le m$. With $c\ge0$ the coefficient $3cs^2\theta_u$ is nonnegative, so $m\le\theta_u\big(c^3+3cs^2(\tfrac12-\gamma)\big)+s^3m$, and with $c^3=c-cs^2$,
$$m\,(1-s^3)\ \le\ \theta_u\big(c+(\tfrac12-3\gamma)\,c\,s^2\big).$$

(4) *The envelope.* $(1-\tfrac{s^2}2)^2=c^2+\tfrac{s^4}4$ gives $c\le1-\tfrac{s^2}2$. If $\gamma\le\tfrac16$, then $c\le1$ gives $(\tfrac12-3\gamma)cs^2\le(\tfrac12-3\gamma)s^2$, so the bracket is at most $1-3\gamma s^2\le1-s^3$, by $s\le3\gamma$. If $\gamma>\tfrac16$, the middle term is at most $0$, so the bracket is at most $1-\tfrac{s^2}2\le1-s^3$, by $s\le\tfrac12$.

(5) $s\le\tfrac12$ makes $1-s^3>0$, so $m\le\theta_u$. $\square$

**What is used**: criticality at $u$, the gap, the total symmetry of $T$, $O(n)$-equivariance, the first variation of distance, and the one fact only a global maximiser supplies, $P(V)\le m$ for every unit $V$. *Not used: [L143](../findings/bounds.md), Bernstein's inequality, any frame, or criticality of $W^\ast$.*

**How it sits against the record.** *It is [L20](route-a.md)'s engine, the self-bootstrap $\gamma_V\le m$, run at any gapped orbit rather than at $\mathbb{CP}^2$ alone.* **Against [L216](#lemma-l216-an-explicit-isolation-radius-at-every-quantitatively-nondegenerate-critical-orbit)** it trades scope for radius: L216 excludes every critical point from its tube, and bounds the cubic remainder by L143 and Bernstein because a critical point supplies no comparison; L217 speaks only of global maximisers, which supply the comparison exactly, and gains a factor $27(1+\sqrt{n-2})/(4\theta_u)$ in the radius, $3\gamma$ against L216's $12\theta_u\gamma/(27(1+\sqrt{n-2}))$, which is $0.3047/0.0132$ at $n=12$. **Against [L147](#lemma-l147-the-tube-radius-tracks-suplvert-prvert-on-betain0tfrac12-and-the-route-ends-at-thetan-1)**, whose $P$-side branch caps a penalised-functional radius at $c_p/S\le c_p/\theta_0=3/(n-2)$ with $S=\sup\lvert P\rvert$ unknown, the scale agrees: the maximiser's value is what that branch lacked.

**What it does not give.** *A global maximiser outside every such tube is untouched, and the tubes about the rigid orbits do not cover the sphere. Conjecture A follows from (a) only under the localisation that some maximiser lies within $\arcsin\frac3{n-2}$ of the balanced orbit, which is [I100](../plan.md)'s target.*

#### Lemma L218, the off-diagonal coupling at a diagonal point is exactly $\sqrt3/2$, so Banach's form of the maximum does not refute Conjecture A through it

*Gate 2 PASSED ([r935](../reviews/REVIEW_r935.md), [r936](../reviews/REVIEW_r936.md)). Derived 2026-09-14 as [I99](../plan.md), the falsifier on route [R25](../routes.md). The block bounds, their equality cases and the comparison with $\Theta_n$ are kernel-checked in [L218.lean](../lean/HamiltonsCubic/Results/L218.lean).*

**Statement.** *Let $n\ge4$ and $\Lambda_n=\sup\{\langle L_DN,N\rangle:D\in\mathcal D_n,\ N\in\mathcal D_n^\perp\cap\mathcal W_n,\ \lVert D\rVert=\lVert N\rVert=1\}$. Then*
$$\Lambda_n=\tfrac{\sqrt3}2,$$
*attained on a four-set block and on a pair block, and $\tfrac{\sqrt3}2<\Theta_n$ at every $n\ge5$ of either parity, with equality at $n=4$.* **So the falsifier $\Lambda_n>\Theta_n$ does not fire at any $n\ge5$.** *By Banach's theorem $\sup_{\lVert x\rVert=1}P(x)=\sup_{\lVert x\rVert=\lVert y\rVert=\lVert z\rVert=1}T(x,y,z)$, a firing would give $M(n)>\Theta_n$, which refutes Conjecture A at $n\ge12$, the conjecture's range, since padded $W_{\mathbb{CP}^2}$ already gives $M(n)\ge\sqrt{3/2}>\Theta_n$ at $n\le11$.*

**Proof.** Write $K$ for $D$'s sectional matrix, hollow and symmetric with zero row sums, with $\sum_{a<b}K_{ab}^2=1$ at unit norm ([conventions.md](conventions.md)). By [Lemma L120](#lemma-l120-l_m-is-block-diagonal-over-the-sign-characters-at-every-diagonal-critical-point), $\mathcal D_n^\perp\cap\mathcal W_n$ is the orthogonal sum of the four-set blocks $V_{ijkl}$ and the pair blocks $V_{jk}$, and $L_D$ preserves each, a support argument needing diagonality alone. So $\langle L_DN,N\rangle$ is a convex combination of the blocks' Rayleigh quotients, and $\Lambda_n$ is the largest block eigenvalue.

*The four-set block.* By [Lemma L121](#lemma-l121-the-4-set-block-its-spectrum-and-one-necessary-condition-per-4-subset), its eigenvalues are $\pm\tfrac12\sqrt{E}$ with $E=A^2+B^2+C^2-AB-BC-CA$, $A=K_{ij}+K_{kl}$, $B=K_{ik}+K_{jl}$, $C=K_{il}+K_{jk}$. Since $E=\tfrac32(A^2+B^2+C^2)-\tfrac12(A+B+C)^2$ and $(x+y)^2\le2(x^2+y^2)$, $E\le3(K_{ij}^2+K_{kl}^2+K_{ik}^2+K_{jl}^2+K_{il}^2+K_{jk}^2)\le3$, so the top eigenvalue is at most $\tfrac{\sqrt3}2$.

*The pair block.* By [Lemma L122](#lemma-l122-the-pair-block-is-a-graph-laplacian-of-the-sectional-matrix), in an orthogonal basis of common norm it is $\tfrac12\big[\mathop{\mathrm{diag}}(d)-\kappa\,\mathrm{Id}+S\big]$ on $\mathbf 1^\perp$, with $d_i=K_{ji}+K_{ki}$, $\kappa=K_{jk}$ and $S$ the hollow submatrix off $\{j,k\}$. At a unit vector $x$ the bracket's form is linear in $K$:
$$\sum_id_ix_i^2-\kappa+x^{\mathsf T}Sx=\langle K,G_x\rangle,$$
where $G_x$ carries $x_i^2$ on the entries $K_{ji}$ and $K_{ki}$, $-1$ on $K_{jk}$, and $2x_ix_l$ on $K_{il}$ for $i<l$. Its squared norm over unordered pairs is $2\sum_ix_i^4+1+4\sum_{i<l}x_i^2x_l^2=2\sum_ix_i^4+1+2\big(1-\sum_ix_i^4\big)=3$ for every unit $x$. Cauchy--Schwarz against $\sum_{a<b}K_{ab}^2=1$ gives at most $\sqrt3$, so the block's top eigenvalue is at most $\tfrac{\sqrt3}2$.

*Equality.* **On a four-set block**, padded $S^2\times S^2$: $K_{12}=K_{34}=2/\sqrt{12}$ and the four cross entries $-1/\sqrt{12}$, unit with zero row sums, so $A=4/\sqrt{12}$, $B=C=-2/\sqrt{12}$ and $E=3$, the value [post F67](fence-posts.md) records. **On a pair block**, $K=G_x/\sqrt3$ for any unit $x\in\mathbf 1^\perp$: it is unit, its rows $j$ and $k$ sum to $\sum_ix_i^2-1=0$ and its row $i$ to $2x_i^2+2x_i\sum_{l\ne i}x_l=0$, and Cauchy--Schwarz is an equality at $x$, which lies in the block. Both need $n\ge4$.

*Against $\Theta_n$.* At even $n$, $\Theta_n^2=\theta_0(n)^2>\tfrac34$ is $(5n-4)(n-4)>0$. At odd $n$, $\Theta_n^2=\tfrac{2(n-1)(n-3)}{(n+1)(n-2)}>\tfrac34$ is $5n^2-29n+30>0$, whose larger root is below $4.5$. Both hold from $n=5$, and $\Theta_4=\tfrac{\sqrt3}2$. $\square$

**What it gives.** *It closes [I99](../plan.md) as a falsifier: through this coupling, Banach's operator form of the maximum reaches exactly $\tfrac{\sqrt3}2$, below $\Theta_n$ at every $n\ge5$, and the margin grows to $\sqrt2-\tfrac{\sqrt3}2$ as $n\to\infty$.* **It is not a route input**: [post F67](fence-posts.md) shows that bounding $\langle L_d\nu,\nu\rangle$ independently of $P(d)$ cannot beat $\Theta_n$ whatever the constant, its ceiling using only $\Lambda_n\ge\Theta_n/2$.

#### Lemma L219, the reflection split: the odd part of a critical point has zero cubic and is a $\tfrac\theta2$-eigenvector of the even part

*Gate 2 PASSED ([r937](../reviews/REVIEW_r937.md), [r938](../reviews/REVIEW_r938.md)). Derived 2026-09-14 as [I102](../plan.md)(a), route [R26](../routes.md), from the [2026-09-14b sweep](../reviews/2026-09-14b-LIT-step8-sweep.md). The algebra is kernel-checked in [L219Statement.lean](../lean/HamiltonsCubic/Results/L219Statement.lean), and the lemma is typed on $\mathcal W_n$ at a coordinate reflection: the split, $P(c)=0$ and $P(b)$ as `L219_op` ([L219Op.lean](../lean/HamiltonsCubic/Results/L219Op.lean), [r1776](../reviews/REVIEW_r1776.md)), the operator identities $b\circ b+c\circ c=\theta b$ and $2b\circ c=\theta c$ as `L219_ops` ([L219Prod.lean](../lean/HamiltonsCubic/Results/L219Prod.lean), [r1778](../reviews/REVIEW_r1778.md)), the reflection preserving $\mathcal W_n$ and diagonality from $n-1$ reflections as `sig_isWeyl2` and `diagonal_iff_even_but_one` ([L219Diag.lean](../lean/HamiltonsCubic/Results/L219Diag.lean), [r1777](../reviews/REVIEW_r1777.md), [r1778](../reviews/REVIEW_r1778.md)), and $\lVert c\rVert^2\le\tfrac34$ at a global maximiser as `odd_mass_max` ([L219Max.lean](../lean/HamiltonsCubic/Results/L219Max.lean), [r1779](../reviews/REVIEW_r1779.md)). At every unit $u$, through the frame change `rotR` of [FrameOp2.lean](../lean/HamiltonsCubic/Results/FrameOp2.lean), the six pairing clauses are `L219_gen` and the bound is `odd_mass_gen` ([L219Gen.lean](../lean/HamiltonsCubic/Results/L219Gen.lean), [r1783](../reviews/REVIEW_r1783.md)). The operator identities at every unit $u$ are `L219_ops_gen`, through `Qop2_rotR`, $Q$ commuting with every frame change on $\mathcal W_n$ ([L219GenOps.lean](../lean/HamiltonsCubic/Results/L219GenOps.lean), Gate 2 [r1784](../reviews/REVIEW_r1784.md), CONFIRMED WITH OBSERVATIONS). The diagonality clause in every orthonormal frame is `diagonal_iff_even_frame` and `diagonal_iff_even_but_one_frame`, through `rotR_Su_col` ($\sigma_{u_k}=U\sigma_{e_k}U^{\mathsf T}$) ([L219FrameDiag.lean](../lean/HamiltonsCubic/Results/L219FrameDiag.lean), Gate 2 [r1785](../reviews/REVIEW_r1785.md), CONFIRMED WITH OBSERVATIONS). The two hypothesis gaps [r1785](../reviews/REVIEW_r1785.md) found are closed: the four clauses that hold at every $W\in\mathcal W_n$ ($\langle b,c\rangle=0$, $T(b,b,c)=0$, $P(c)=0$ and the split) are `L219_split_gen`, at every unit $u$ with no criticality hypothesis, and $\lVert c_u\rVert^2\le\tfrac34$ at every unit $u$ and every global maximiser at $n\ge4$ is `odd_mass_gen_global` ([L219GenMax.lean](../lean/HamiltonsCubic/Results/L219GenMax.lean)), with no other hypothesis. [r1786](../reviews/REVIEW_r1786.md), CONFIRMED WITH OBSERVATIONS, maps all ten clauses of L219 to theorems, the bound's $n\ge4$ removing nothing since $\mathcal W_n=0$ at $n\le3$, so the lemma is typed in full.* **The parity mechanism is [L123](#lemma-l123-a-zero-diagonal-operator-contributes-nothing-to-a-single-character-rayleigh-quotient)'s character argument at one reflection, with [L214](#lemma-l214-the-frame-split-two-exact-relations-in-every-frame-and-one-inequality-under-the-second-order-cap)'s shape, and the even subspace is the C3 slice $\mathrm{fix}(\Lambda^2\sigma)$ of [graded-half.md](graded-half.md) at an involution with one negative direction.** *New here: the split formula, $P(c)=0$, the $\tfrac\theta2$-eigenvector equation and the value of $P(b)$.*

**Statement.** *Let $u\in\mathbb R^n$ be a unit vector, $\sigma_u\in O(n)$ the reflection in $u^\perp$, and split $W\in\mathcal W_n$ as $W=b+c$ with $\sigma_ub=b$ and $\sigma_uc=-c$. Then*
$$P(W)=P(b)+3\langle L_bc,c\rangle,\qquad P(c)=0,\qquad\langle b\circ b,c\rangle=0 .$$
*If $W$ is a critical point, $Q(W)=\theta W$, then*
$$b\circ b+c\circ c=\theta b,\qquad 2\,b\circ c=\theta c,\qquad\langle L_bc,c\rangle=\tfrac\theta2\lVert c\rVert^2,\qquad P(b)=\theta\big(\lVert b\rVert^2-\tfrac12\lVert c\rVert^2\big).$$
**And $W$ is coordinate-diagonal in an orthonormal frame $(e_1,\dots,e_n)$ exactly when its $\sigma_{e_k}$-odd part vanishes for $k=1,\dots,n-1$**, the last reflection being redundant since $-I$ acts trivially on $\mathcal W_n$. **At a unit global maximiser, $\lVert c_u\rVert^2\le\tfrac34$ for every unit $u$.**

**Proof.** $O(n)$ acts on $\mathcal W_n$ by isometric algebra automorphisms, so $\sigma=\sigma_u$ satisfies $\sigma(x\circ y)=\sigma x\circ\sigma y$ and $\langle\sigma x,\sigma y\rangle=\langle x,y\rangle$, and $\sigma^2=1$. Hence the even and odd parts are orthogonal, $\sigma(b\circ b)=b\circ b$ and $\sigma(c\circ c)=c\circ c$ are even, and $\sigma(b\circ c)=-b\circ c$ is odd.

1. **The split.** Expanding $P(b+c)=\langle(b+c)\circ(b+c),b+c\rangle$ gives six terms. $\langle b\circ b,c\rangle$, $\langle b\circ c,b\rangle$ and $\langle c\circ c,c\rangle$ pair an even vector with an odd one and vanish, which is $\langle b\circ b,c\rangle=0$ and $P(c)=0$. The remaining three are $P(b)+\langle c\circ c,b\rangle+2\langle b\circ c,c\rangle=P(b)+3\langle b\circ c,c\rangle$ by total symmetry of $\langle x\circ y,z\rangle$.
2. **At a critical point.** $Q(W)=b\circ b+c\circ c+2\,b\circ c$, whose even part is $b\circ b+c\circ c$ and odd part $2\,b\circ c$, so $Q(W)=\theta W$ splits into the two displayed equations. Pairing the odd one with $c$ gives $\langle L_bc,c\rangle=\tfrac\theta2\lVert c\rVert^2$. Pairing the even one with $b$ gives $P(b)+\langle b\circ c,c\rangle=\theta\lVert b\rVert^2$, hence $P(b)=\theta(\lVert b\rVert^2-\tfrac12\lVert c\rVert^2)$.
3. **Diagonality.** The reflections $\sigma_{e_k}$ generate the sign group $\Gamma$ of the frame, and $\mathcal D_n$ is exactly the $\Gamma$-fixed subspace ([L214](#lemma-l214-the-frame-split-two-exact-relations-in-every-frame-and-one-inequality-under-the-second-order-cap), from [L120](#lemma-l120-l_m-is-block-diagonal-over-the-sign-characters-at-every-diagonal-critical-point)). So $W\in\mathcal D_n$ iff $\sigma_{e_k}W=W$ for every $k$, iff every odd part vanishes, and $\sigma_{e_1}\cdots\sigma_{e_n}=-I$ acts trivially, so $n-1$ of them suffice.
4. **The bound at a maximiser.** On the plane $\{\alpha b+\beta c\}$ at unit norm, $\beta^2s^2=1-\alpha^2(1-s^2)$, and the two relations give $P=\tfrac\theta2(3\alpha-\alpha^3)$, which exceeds $\theta$ for $\alpha<-2$. The admissible range reaches $\alpha=-(1-s^2)^{-1/2}$, so a global maximiser has $(1-s^2)^{-1/2}\le2$, that is $s^2\le\tfrac34$. $\square$

**In block terms**, for $u=e_n$, labelling blocks by their sign character: the even part is $\mathcal D_n$, the pair blocks of character $\varepsilon_j\varepsilon_k$ with $j,k<n$ and the four-set blocks of character $\chi_Q$ with $n\notin Q$, and the odd part is the pair blocks of character $\varepsilon_j\varepsilon_n$ and the four-set blocks with $n\in Q$. *A block's label names its character, not the indices of its basis tensors: the even block $V_{jk}$ contains $e_{nj}\odot e_{nk}$.*

**What it gives.** *The root node of Problem P, that a global maximiser is coordinate-diagonal, becomes $n-1$ applications of one statement: a maximiser on the fixed subalgebra of reflections in $e_1,\dots,e_k$ is even under $\sigma_u$ for SOME unit $u\perp e_1,\dots,e_k$.* *With the frame fixed in advance the statement is false, since $O(1)\times O(n-1)$ fixes nothing in $\mathcal W_n$ ([r937](../reviews/REVIEW_r937.md)).* The frame split [L214](#lemma-l214-the-frame-split-two-exact-relations-in-every-frame-and-one-inequality-under-the-second-order-cap) and the four-set split of [I101](../plan.md) have the same shape with a third term $P(N)$, which here vanishes. **H3 on the plane $\{b,c\}$ is automatic**, reducing to $\theta\ge0$ with slack $\tfrac\theta2s^4$, **and H4 on the same plane gives only $s^2\le\tfrac34$**, so the lemma is a calculus rather than a localisation: the one [I102](../plan.md)(b) and (c) work in.

**Gate 1** (screen, [sweep](../reviews/2026-09-14b-LIT-step8-sweep.md) batch 3). *At $n=8$, at Fox's $\sqrt{3/2}$ and at $S^4\times S^4$ in a random frame, reflection in $e_0$: $P(c)=0$ exactly, the split to $5\times10^{-6}$, $P(b)/\theta=1-\tfrac32s^2$ at unit norm to four digits, and $2b\circ c=\theta c$ to $3\times10^{-7}$, the product staying in $\mathcal W_n$ to $6\times10^{-16}$ as [L209](curvature-invariants.md) requires.*

#### Lemma L220, the diagonal theorem on a four-set subalgebra: a critical point with a four-set component has $\theta\le\sqrt{3/2}$

*Gate 2 PASSED ([r939](../reviews/REVIEW_r939.md), [r940](../reviews/REVIEW_r940.md), items 1 to 8 CONFIRMED by both). Derived 2026-09-14 as [I101](../plan.md), the single-four-set case of the far region, after four failed attempts recorded in the [I100 derivation](../reviews/2026-09-14-I100-derivation.md). The scalar chain and both polynomial inequalities are kernel-checked in [L220Statement.lean](../lean/HamiltonsCubic/Results/L220Statement.lean). Steps 1 and 3 at the operators are in [BlockSub.lean](../lean/HamiltonsCubic/Results/BlockSub.lean), for every nonempty index set: `S_mul` and `L220_sub`, $S_Q$ closed under $\circ$, `comp_zero_of_fixed` and `weyl_fixed_iff`, $S_t\cap\mathcal W_n$ exactly the fixed space of $\ker\chi_t$ in $\mathcal W_n$ (step 1's identification), and `L220_split`, $D\circ D+\nu\circ\nu=\theta D$ and $2D\circ\nu=\theta\nu$ with no Weyl, unit or $\theta>0$ hypothesis ([r1790](../reviews/REVIEW_r1790.md), CONFIRMED WITH OBSERVATIONS), and step 1's maximiser clause is `L220_step1_crit` through `crit_sub` in [SubFermat.lean](../lean/HamiltonsCubic/Results/SubFermat.lean) ([r1792](../reviews/REVIEW_r1792.md), CONFIRMED WITH OBSERVATIONS), with the components Weyl by `split_weyl` in [CompChar.lean](../lean/HamiltonsCubic/Results/CompChar.lean). Step 2's square is typed: `nu_sq` in [FourProd.lean](../lean/HamiltonsCubic/Results/FourProd.lean), every Weyl $\nu$ of character $\chi_Q$ with signed coordinates $(a,b,c)$, $a+b+c=0$, has $\nu\circ\nu=\Pi_Q(2a^2-b^2-c^2,\,2b^2-c^2-a^2,\,2c^2-a^2-b^2)$ ([r1830](../reviews/REVIEW_r1830.md), CONFIRMED), on `Q_sig` in [FourSquare.lean](../lean/HamiltonsCubic/Results/FourSquare.lean) ([r1826](../reviews/REVIEW_r1826.md)) and `four_eq_sig` in [FourIdent.lean](../lean/HamiltonsCubic/Results/FourIdent.lean) ([r1829](../reviews/REVIEW_r1829.md)), both CONFIRMED WITH OBSERVATIONS. *The signed coordinates are `spanVec`'s, whose middle term is $e_{ki}\odot e_{jl}$, which makes $a+b+c=0$ the Bianchi condition; in the unsigned $e_{ik}$ basis it reads $a-b+c=0$.* The rest of step 2 is in [FourPi.lean](../lean/HamiltonsCubic/Results/FourPi.lean): `PiQ_mul`, $\Pi_Q$ closed under $\circ$ (the three values of a product sum to $(x+y+z)(x'+y'+z')$), `PiQ_weight`, $\Pi_Q\subset\mathcal D_n$, and `Dnu_char`, $D\circ\nu$ of character $\chi_Q$ (Gate 2 [r1831](../reviews/REVIEW_r1831.md), CONFIRMED WITH OBSERVATIONS), so **step 2 is complete on the operators**. Step 4 is in [FourStep4.lean](../lean/HamiltonsCubic/Results/FourStep4.lean): `Lr_zero`, $r\circ\nu=0$ at a Weyl $r$ of character $1$ orthogonal to $\Pi_Q$, `mixed_sq`, $\nu\circ\nu'\in\Pi_Q$, and `Lq_eigen`, $2q\circ\nu=\theta\nu$ (Gate 2 [r1832](../reviews/REVIEW_r1832.md), CONFIRMED WITH OBSERVATIONS, its one REFUTED AS STATED item on the INDEX row). Step 5 is `step5` in [FourStep5.lean](../lean/HamiltonsCubic/Results/FourStep5.lean), $\theta^2=3\lVert q\rVert^2$, and step 6, step 7 and the norm split are `step6`, `step7`, `norm_split` and `P_PiQ` in [FourStep67.lean](../lean/HamiltonsCubic/Results/FourStep67.lean) (Gate 2 [r1838](../reviews/REVIEW_r1838.md), CONFIRMED WITH OBSERVATIONS). Step 8 is `step8` in [FourStep8.lean](../lean/HamiltonsCubic/Results/FourStep8.lean), $3\kappa\le\theta\lVert r\rVert^2$, and **the inequality is proved on the operators**: `L220_bound` in [FourL220.lean](../lean/HamiltonsCubic/Results/FourL220.lean), $\theta^2\le\tfrac32$ at every unit $W=D+\nu$ with $D$ Weyl of character $1$, $0\ne\nu$ Weyl of character $\chi_Q$, $Q(W)=\theta W$, $\theta>0$, through `proj_PiQ`, with `L220_eq` giving $D\in\Pi_Q$ and $\lVert\nu\rVert^2=\tfrac12$ at $\theta^2=\tfrac32$ (Gate 2 [r1839](../reviews/REVIEW_r1839.md), CONFIRMED WITH OBSERVATIONS). **The $n\ge12$ clause and the equality case are proved on the operators**: `L220_sup_le` and `L220_attained_diag` in [FourN12.lean](../lean/HamiltonsCubic/Results/FourN12.lean), $P\le\Theta$ on the unit sphere of $\mathcal S_Q\cap\mathcal W_n$ for every $\Theta>0$ with $\Theta^2>\tfrac32$ bounding $\mathcal D_n$ as L57 does, $P=\Theta$ forcing $W\in\mathcal D_n$, through a compactness maximiser (`exists_SW_maximiser`), `L220_step1_crit` and `L220_bound`, with instances `L220_n12_even` ($\Theta=\theta_0(n)$, $n\ge12$) and `L220_n12_odd` (odd $n\ge13$); and in [FourEq.lean](../lean/HamiltonsCubic/Results/FourEq.lean) `L220_eq_shape` ($D=\Pi_Q$ of a permutation of $\tfrac\theta6(2,-1,-1)$, $\lVert\nu\rVert^2=\tfrac12$), `converse`, and `L220_witness`, a unit $W$ with $\nu\ne0$ and $Q(W)=\sqrt{3/2}\,W$, so the bound is sharp (Gate 2 [r1840](../reviews/REVIEW_r1840.md), CONFIRMED WITH OBSERVATIONS). The supremum clause, with $\Theta_n$ attained by the balanced product, is `L220_sup_even` and `L220_sup_odd` in [FourSup.lean](../lean/HamiltonsCubic/Results/FourSup.lean), and `L220_eq_points` in [FourEqNu.lean](../lean/HamiltonsCubic/Results/FourEqNu.lean) lists the equality points as the three placements below (Gate 2 [r1841](../reviews/REVIEW_r1841.md), CONFIRMED WITH OBSERVATIONS). `converse_rows` there, through `converse_gen`, makes every placement an equality point, so **every clause of the Statement is proved on the operators** (Gate 2 [r1844](../reviews/REVIEW_r1844.md), CONFIRMED WITH OBSERVATIONS). Naming the six as padded $W_{\mathbb{CP}^2}$ is the record's description, argued in step 9.*

**Statement.** *Let $n\ge5$, $Q=\{i,j,k,l\}$, $V_Q$ the four-set block of character $\chi_Q$ and $\mathcal S_Q=\mathcal D_n\oplus V_Q$. Every unit critical point $W=D+\nu$ of $P$ in $\mathcal S_Q$ with $\nu\in V_Q$, $\nu\ne0$ and $\theta>0$ has*
$$\theta\le\sqrt{3/2},$$
*with equality exactly at $r=0$, $q=t\,p$, $\nu=\pm t\,p$, where $t=1/\sqrt{24}$ and $p$ is a permutation of $(2,-1,-1)$ in the pairing coordinates below, the six placements of padded $W_{\mathbb{CP}^2}$ on $Q$. Hence at $n\ge12$, $\sup P=\Theta_n$ on the unit sphere of $\mathcal S_Q$, attained only on $\mathcal D_n$.*

**Setting.** $\Pi_Q\subset\mathcal D_n$ is the plane of sectional matrices supported on $Q$, $K_{ij}=K_{kl}=x$, $K_{ik}=K_{jl}=y$, $K_{il}=K_{jk}=z$ with $x+y+z=0$, unit when $2(x^2+y^2+z^2)=1$. Write $D=q+r$ with $q\in\Pi_Q$, $r\in\mathcal D_n\ominus\Pi_Q$, and in the pairing basis of $V_Q$ with signed coordinates $\nu=a\,e_{ij}\odot e_{kl}+b\,e_{ik}\odot e_{jl}+c\,e_{il}\odot e_{jk}$, $a+b+c=0$.

**Proof.**

1. **$\mathcal S_Q$ is a subalgebra and its maximum is critical.** $\mathcal S_Q$ is the fixed space of $\ker\chi_Q\subset\Gamma$, since the only characters of [L120](#lemma-l120-l_m-is-block-diagonal-over-the-sign-characters-at-every-diagonal-critical-point)'s decomposition trivial on $\ker\chi_Q$ are $1$ and $\chi_Q$: a four-set character $\chi_{Q'}$, $Q'\ne Q$, is $-1$ on the single flip at an index of $Q'\setminus Q$, which lies in $\ker\chi_Q$, and a pair character $\varepsilon_a\varepsilon_b$ is $-1$ on the flip at $a$ when $a\notin Q$, or on $\varepsilon_a\varepsilon_c$ with $c\in Q\setminus\{a,b\}$ when $a,b\in Q$. A fixed space of automorphisms is a subalgebra, so the maximum of $P$ on its unit sphere satisfies $Q(W)=\theta W$ in $\mathcal W_n$. *Where the maximiser has $\nu=0$ it is diagonal and [L57](problem.md) bounds it by $\Theta_n$.*
2. **Three products.** $\Pi_Q$ is a subalgebra of $\mathcal D_n$, since a hollow matrix supported on $Q$ with zero row sums has $S^{\circ2}+S^2$ supported on $Q$ off the diagonal with zero row sums. $\nu\circ\nu\in\Pi_Q$, with $x=2a^2-b^2-c^2$ and cyclically: it has character $\chi_Q^2=1$, so it lies in $\mathcal D_n$, in $(\nu\#\nu)_{\alpha\alpha}$ a pair $\alpha$ leaving $Q$ forces a factor of $\nu$ outside its support, and on $Q$ the pattern is $(\nu\#\nu)_{ij,ij}=2bc$ plus $(\nu^2)_{ij,ij}=a^2$. And $D\circ\nu\in V_Q$ by L120.
3. **Split the equation.** $Q(W)=D\circ D+2D\circ\nu+\nu\circ\nu$, so $D\circ D+\nu\circ\nu=\theta D$ and $L_D\nu=\tfrac\theta2\nu$.
4. **$L_r\nu=0$.** $L_r\nu\in V_Q$, and $\langle L_r\nu,\nu'\rangle=\langle r,\nu\circ\nu'\rangle=0$ for $\nu'\in V_Q$, as $\nu\circ\nu'\in\Pi_Q\perp r$. So $L_q\nu=\tfrac\theta2\nu$.
5. **The eigenvalue fixes $\lVert q\rVert$.** By [L121](#lemma-l121-the-4-set-block-its-spectrum-and-one-necessary-condition-per-4-subset) $L_q$ on $V_Q$ has eigenvalues $\pm\tfrac12\sqrt E$ with $A=2x$, $B=2y$, $C=2z$, so $E=4(x^2+y^2+z^2-xy-yz-zx)=6(x^2+y^2+z^2)=3\lVert q\rVert^2$, using $xy+yz+zx=-\tfrac12(x^2+y^2+z^2)$. With $\theta>0$ and $\nu\ne0$, $\lVert q\rVert^2=\theta^2/3$.
6. **Pair the $\mathcal D_n$ equation with $q$.** $\langle D\circ D,q\rangle=P(q)+2\langle q\circ r,q\rangle+\langle r\circ r,q\rangle$ with $\langle q\circ r,q\rangle=\langle r,q\circ q\rangle=0$, $\langle r\circ r,q\rangle=\langle L_qr,r\rangle=:\kappa$, and $\langle\nu\circ\nu,q\rangle=\langle L_q\nu,\nu\rangle=\tfrac\theta2\lVert\nu\rVert^2$, so $P(q)+\kappa+\tfrac\theta2\lVert\nu\rVert^2=\tfrac{\theta^3}3$.
7. **$P$ on $\Pi_Q$.** $P(q)=\sum_{a<b}K_{ab}^3+3\sum_{\text{triangles}}K_{ab}K_{bc}K_{ca}=2(x^3+y^3+z^3)+12xyz=18xyz$, and $x+y+z=0$ gives $(x^2+y^2+z^2)^3\ge54x^2y^2z^2$, so $P(q)\le\tfrac{\sqrt3}2\lVert q\rVert^3=\tfrac{\theta^3}6$, equality at the $S^2\times S^2$ shape.
8. **The coupling.** For unit $\hat q=(x,y,z)$ and $r\perp\Pi_Q$, split $r$ into its $Q$-internal entries $p,p',a',b',c',d'$ on the pairs $ij,kl,ik,jl,il,jk$, its columns $c_m=(r_{am})_{a\in Q}$ for $m\notin Q$, and the rest. Then $2\langle L_{\hat q}r,r\rangle=2[x(p^2+p'^2)+y(a'^2+b'^2)+z(c'^2+d'^2)]+2\Sigma+\sum_mc_m^{\mathsf T}\hat qc_m$ with $\Sigma=z(p+p')(a'+b')+y(p+p')(c'+d')+x(a'+b')(c'+d')$, the entries outside $Q$'s rows not entering. Orthogonality to $\Pi_Q$ is $p+p'=a'+b'=c'+d'=:s$, so $\Sigma=s^2(x+y+z)=0$, and $\hat q$ has spectrum $\{0,2x,2y,2z\}$ on $\mathbb R^Q$. So $\kappa\le\max(x,y,z)\lVert q\rVert\lVert r\rVert^2\le\tfrac1{\sqrt3}\lVert q\rVert\lVert r\rVert^2=\tfrac\theta3\lVert r\rVert^2$.
9. **Arithmetic.** Steps 6 to 8 give $\tfrac{\theta^3}6-\tfrac\theta2\lVert\nu\rVert^2\le\tfrac\theta3\lVert r\rVert^2$. With $\lVert r\rVert^2=1-\tfrac{\theta^2}3-\lVert\nu\rVert^2$ this is $\tfrac5{18}\theta^2\le\tfrac13+\tfrac16\lVert\nu\rVert^2$, and $\lVert\nu\rVert^2\le1-\tfrac{\theta^2}3$ gives $\theta^2\le\tfrac32$. Equality forces $r=0$, $\lVert\nu\rVert^2=\tfrac12$ and the $S^2\times S^2$ shape, and conversely $q=t(2,-1,-1)$, $\nu=\pm t(2,-1,-1)$ with $t=+\tfrac1{\sqrt{24}}$ satisfies both equations of step 3 at $\theta=\sqrt{3/2}$ (at $t<0$ the eigenvalue equation $2q\circ\nu=\theta\nu$ gives $-\sqrt{3/2}$, [r1835](../reviews/REVIEW_r1835.md) O4), and the other two placements follow by relabelling $Q$, swapping $j,k$ exchanging the first two pairings and swapping $j,l$ the first and third. The six are padded $W_{\mathbb{CP}^2}$ on $Q$: a permutation of $Q$ permutes the three pairings, and the flip at one index of $Q$ fixes $D$ and negates $\nu$, $\chi_Q$ being $-1$ on it. At $n\ge12$, $\Theta_n>\sqrt{3/2}$ (and $\Theta_{11}<\sqrt{3/2}$), $\Theta_n$ is attained in $\mathcal D_n$ by the balanced product ([Nienhaus Proposition 2.25](problem.md)), and L57 bounds $\mathcal D_n$, so every maximiser on $\mathcal S_Q$ has $\nu=0$. $\square$

**Corollary, inequality (J).** *For every unit $d\in\mathcal D_n$, $n\ge12$, with top $V_Q$-eigenvalue $e=e(d)>\Theta_n/2$: $P(d)<3e-4e^3/\Theta_n^2$.* **Proof.** If not, take $\nu$ the unit top eigenvector of $L_d$ on $V_Q$ and $c=\Theta_n/(2e)<1$. Then $P(cd+s\nu)=c^3P(d)+3cs^2e=(P(d)-3e)c^3+3ec\ge-\tfrac{4e^3}{\Theta_n^2}c^3+3ec=\Theta_n$, using $T(d,d,\nu)=0$ and $P(\nu)=0$, a unit point of $\mathcal S_Q$ with $\nu$-component $s\ne0$ at or above $\Theta_n$, which the lemma excludes. $\square$ *Added at the fold: the reviewers found the block claiming (J) without this step.*

**What it gives.** *It closes [I101](../plan.md) and, through the corollary, inequality (J): no global maximiser at $n\ge12$ has a NONZERO off-diagonal part, in any frame, lying inside a single four-set block.* **The lever is step 5**: the eigenvalue condition of the critical point pins $\lVert q\rVert$ to $\theta/\sqrt3$, so both bounds of steps 7 and 8 become functions of $\theta$ with their equality at the same point, and nothing is bounded at two different vectors, which is what [post F67](fence-posts.md) forbids. *Four whole-sphere attempts failed where this critical-point argument closes.*

**Gate 1** (screen). *Ascent restricted to $\mathcal S_Q$, 60 starts each at $n=8$ and $n=12$: every converged critical point with $\nu\ne0$ sits at $\theta=1.224745=\sqrt{3/2}$ with $\lVert\nu\rVert^2=0.5000$, and every other has $\nu=0$ ($\Theta_{12}$, $S^5\times S^7$ and lower at $n=12$). The whole-sphere screen of (J) found its extreme at the same point.*

#### Lemma L221, L220 on several four-set blocks: disjoint families, and two four-sets sharing two indices

*Gate 2 PASSED ([r941](../reviews/REVIEW_r941.md), [r942](../reviews/REVIEW_r942.md), all eight items CONFIRMED by both). Derived 2026-09-14 as [I104](../plan.md) from [L220](#lemma-l220-the-diagonal-theorem-on-a-four-set-subalgebra-a-critical-point-with-a-four-set-component-has-thetalesqrt32), in the [mixed-case note](../reviews/2026-09-14-I100-mixed-case.md). The norm identity and the scalar steps are kernel-checked in [L221Statement.lean](../lean/HamiltonsCubic/Results/L221Statement.lean), and at the operators in [L221Ops.lean](../lean/HamiltonsCubic/Results/L221Ops.lean): `L221b_split`, (b)'s step 1 four equations at any non-nested $Q_1,Q_2$, `L221b_tau_zero`, $\nu_3=0\Rightarrow\nu_1\circ\nu_2=0$, and `disjoint_prod_zero`, (a)'s $\nu_i\circ\nu_j=0$ at disjoint four-sets ([r1791](../reviews/REVIEW_r1791.md), CONFIRMED WITH OBSERVATIONS). (b)'s step 1 is proved in full in [L221Crit.lean](../lean/HamiltonsCubic/Results/L221Crit.lean): `K_mul`, $\mathcal D_n\oplus V_{Q_1}\oplus V_{Q_2}\oplus V_{Q_3}$ closed under $\circ$, `SK_Qop2`, its intersection with $\mathcal W_n$ closed under $Q$, and `L221b_step1`, a local maximiser of $P$ on that sphere is critical in $\mathcal W_n$ with $\theta=P(W)$ and satisfies the four equations. (a)'s structure for $m$ pairwise disjoint four-sets is in [L221a.lean](../lean/HamiltonsCubic/Results/L221a.lean): `multi_split_zero`, `M_mul`, the space a subalgebra, `L221a_split`, $D\circ D+\sum\nu_i\circ\nu_i=\theta D$ and $2D\circ\nu_i=\theta\nu_i$, and `L221a_crit`, a local maximiser on its Weyl part's sphere is critical with $\theta=P(W)$ ([r1792](../reviews/REVIEW_r1792.md), CONFIRMED WITH OBSERVATIONS). At a local maximiser both parts hold with criticality derived, `L221a_at_max` and `L221b_at_max` in [L221Max.lean](../lean/HamiltonsCubic/Results/L221Max.lean) ([r1874](../reviews/REVIEW_r1874.md)), and every character component of both spaces is Weyl by `SK_components` and `SM_components`, through `split_weyl` in [CompChar.lean](../lean/HamiltonsCubic/Results/CompChar.lean). (a) is proved on the operators as `L221a_bound` in [L221aChain.lean](../lean/HamiltonsCubic/Results/L221aChain.lean): L220's chain with the extra terms $\langle\nu_j\circ\nu_j,q_i\rangle$ killed by $\nu_j\circ\nu_j\in\Pi_{Q_j}\perp\Pi_{Q_i}$ (`nunu_orth`) and the norm weakened as the proof says (`L220_bound_extra`), $\theta^2\le\tfrac32$ ([r1870](../reviews/REVIEW_r1870.md)). (b) is proved on the operators as `L221b_bound` in [L221bChain.lean](../lean/HamiltonsCubic/Results/L221bChain.lean), with L220's step 5 used as the Rayleigh bound `rayleigh_sq` and the cross-term angle as `angle12`, `angle13`, `angle23` in [L221bRay.lean](../lean/HamiltonsCubic/Results/L221bRay.lean).*

**Statement.** *Let $n\ge6$ and $\theta>0$.*
- **(a) Disjoint families.** *If $Q_1,\dots,Q_m$ are pairwise disjoint four-sets, every unit critical point of $P$ in $\mathcal D_n\oplus V_{Q_1}\oplus\cdots\oplus V_{Q_m}$ with some nonzero block component has $\theta\le\sqrt{3/2}$.*
- **(b) Two four-sets sharing two indices.** *If $\lvert Q_1\cap Q_2\rvert=2$ and $Q_3=Q_1\triangle Q_2$, every unit critical point $W=D+\nu_1+\nu_2+\nu_3$ of $P$ in $\mathcal S=\mathcal D_n\oplus V_{Q_1}\oplus V_{Q_2}\oplus V_{Q_3}$ with at least two nonzero $\nu_i$ and $\tau=\langle\nu_1\circ\nu_2,\nu_3\rangle\le0$ has $\theta^2\le\tfrac65$.* (If $\nu_3=0$, then $\tau=0$.) *A unit critical point in $\mathcal S$ means $Q(W)=\theta W$ with $W\in\mathcal S\cap\mathcal W_n$, the form `L221a_split` and `L221b_split` take; since $\mathcal S\cap\mathcal W_n$ is closed under $Q$, it is equivalent to criticality of $P$ on that space's unit sphere, which the Lean derives only at a local maximiser (`L221a_crit`, `L221b_step1`, [r1792](../reviews/REVIEW_r1792.md) O3).*

**Proof of (a).** By duality in the sign group, the characters trivial on $\bigcap_i\ker\chi_{Q_i}$ form the group generated by the $\chi_{Q_i}$. Among L120's characters these are $1$ and the $\chi_{Q_i}$, as the explicit witnesses confirm: a four-set character $\chi_{Q'}$ with $Q'$ not among the $Q_i$ is $-1$ on the flip at an index of $Q'$ outside $\bigcup_iQ_i$ when there is one, and otherwise on $\varepsilon_a\varepsilon_b$ with $a\in Q'\cap Q_i$, $b\in Q_i\setminus Q'$ for an $i$ with $Q'\cap Q_i$ a proper nonempty subset, a pair character $\varepsilon_a\varepsilon_b$ is $-1$ on the flip at $a$ when $a\notin\bigcup_iQ_i$ and on $\varepsilon_a\varepsilon_c$ with $c\in Q_i\setminus\{a,b\}$ when $a\in Q_i$, every such flip lying in each $\ker\chi_{Q_j}$, and $\chi_{Q_i}\chi_{Q_j}$ for $i\ne j$ is the character of an eight-element set, which no block carries. So the space is a subalgebra, $\nu_i\circ\nu_j=0$ for $i\ne j$, and $\nu_i\circ\nu_i\in\Pi_{Q_i}$. Fix $i$ with $\nu_i\ne0$, write $D=q_i+r_i$ with $r_i\perp\Pi_{Q_i}$, and run L220's steps 3 to 9 for the block $V_{Q_i}$: step 4 holds as $\langle r_i\circ\nu_i,\nu'\rangle=\langle r_i,\nu_i\circ\nu'\rangle=0$, step 6 gains the terms $\langle\nu_j\circ\nu_j,q_i\rangle$ for $j\ne i$, which vanish since $\Pi_{Q_j}\perp\Pi_{Q_i}$ by disjoint support, and step 9 runs with $\lVert r_i\rVert^2=1-\tfrac{\theta^2}3-\sum_j\lVert\nu_j\rVert^2\le1-\tfrac{\theta^2}3-\lVert\nu_i\rVert^2$, so step 9 runs verbatim. $\square$

**Proof of (b).**
1. **Equations.** By duality the characters trivial on $\ker\chi_{Q_1}\cap\ker\chi_{Q_2}$ are the group generated by $\chi_{Q_1},\chi_{Q_2}$, which is $\{1,\chi_{Q_1},\chi_{Q_2},\chi_{Q_3}\}$, so $\mathcal S$ is a subalgebra and criticality splits into $D\circ D+\sum_i\nu_i\circ\nu_i=\theta D$ and $2D\circ\nu_i+2\nu_j\circ\nu_k=\theta\nu_i$ for $\{i,j,k\}=\{1,2,3\}$. With $\nu_3=0$ the $\chi_{Q_3}$ equation forces $\nu_1\circ\nu_2=0$, so $\tau=0$.
2. **The pinning inequality.** With $q_i$ the projection of $D$ on $\Pi_{Q_i}$, L220's step 4 gives $\langle D\circ\nu_i,\nu_i\rangle=\langle L_{q_i}\nu_i,\nu_i\rangle$, and pairing the $\chi_{Q_i}$ equation with $\nu_i$ gives $\langle L_{q_i}\nu_i,\nu_i\rangle=\tfrac\theta2N_i-\tau$, $N_i=\lVert\nu_i\rVert^2$. With L220's step 5 and $\tau\le0$: $\tfrac{\sqrt3}2u_iN_i\ge\tfrac\theta2N_i$, so $u_i=\lVert q_i\rVert\ge\theta/\sqrt3$ whenever $\nu_i\ne0$.
3. **Two constants.** With the norm $\lVert\nu\rVert^2=2(a^2+b^2+c^2)$ on $V_Q$, $\lVert\nu\circ\nu\rVert=\tfrac{\sqrt3}2\lVert\nu\rVert^2$, the identity $2\sum_{\mathrm{cyc}}(2a^2-b^2-c^2)^2=\tfrac34\big(2(a^2+b^2+c^2)\big)^2$ at $a+b+c=0$. And unit patterns on $Q_i$ and $Q_j$ share exactly one pair, whose entry $x$ satisfies $3x^2\le2(x^2+y^2+z^2)=1$ under $x+y+z=0$, so it has square at most $\tfrac13$ on each, so $\lvert\langle\hat q_i,\hat q_j\rangle\rvert\le\tfrac13$. Hence $\lvert\langle\nu_j\circ\nu_j,q_i\rangle\rvert\le\tfrac{\sqrt3}2N_j\cdot\tfrac13u_i=\tfrac{u_i}{2\sqrt3}N_j$.
4. **The per-block chain.** Pairing the $\mathcal D_n$ equation with $q_i$, $r_i=D-q_i$, $t=\lVert D\rVert^2=1-N$:
$$\theta u_i^2=P(q_i)+\langle L_{q_i}r_i,r_i\rangle+\big(\tfrac\theta2N_i-\tau\big)+\sum_{j\ne i}\langle\nu_j\circ\nu_j,q_i\rangle\le\tfrac{\sqrt3}2u_i^3+\tfrac{u_i}{\sqrt3}(t-u_i^2)+\tfrac{\sqrt3}2u_iN_i+\tfrac{u_i}{2\sqrt3}(N-N_i),$$
using L220's steps 7 and 8 and step 2's upper bound $\tfrac{\sqrt3}2u_iN_i$ on the pinning term. Dividing by $u_i>0$: $\theta u_i-\tfrac{u_i^2}{2\sqrt3}\le\tfrac1{\sqrt3}\big(1-\tfrac N2+N_i\big)$. The left side increases for $u_i\le\sqrt3\theta$, which holds as $u_i\le1<\sqrt3\theta$ at every $\theta>\tfrac1{\sqrt3}$ (and $\theta^2\le\tfrac13<\tfrac65$ otherwise), so $u_i\ge\theta/\sqrt3$ gives $\tfrac56\theta^2\le1-\tfrac N2+N_i$.
5. **Sum.** Over the two or three blocks with $\nu_i\ne0$ (a block with $\nu_i=0$ contributes $N_i=0$ and is not summed): with three, $\tfrac52\theta^2\le3-\tfrac N2\le3$, and with two, $\tfrac53\theta^2\le2-N+N=2$. Either way $\theta^2\le\tfrac65$. $\square$

**What it gives and what remains.** *At $n\ge12$, (a) and (b), with L220 for one component, settle every stratum of the far region whose block part lies in disjoint four-set blocks, or in two four-sets sharing two indices with $\tau\le0$.* **Open**: (b) with $\tau>0$, where by screen $\lvert\tau\rvert\le\tfrac12\sqrt{N_1N_2N_3}$ and the pinning loses up to $\tfrac12\sqrt{N_i}$, four-sets sharing three indices, and pair blocks. *Gate 1 (screen): restricted ascent and a least-squares search for critical points of every index at $n=8$ found no critical point with two or more block components above $\theta=0.66$, and ascent at $n=8$ and $12$ found every block-carrying maximum at L220's $\sqrt{3/2}$ with one component.*

#### Lemma L222, the pair-block calculus: $\nu\circ\nu$, the pinning, the swap parity and the rotation angle on $\mathcal D_n\oplus V_{jk}$

*Gate 2: [r943](../reviews/REVIEW_r943.md) and [r944](../reviews/REVIEW_r944.md) on identical briefs, items 1 to 6 CONFIRMED by both, and the paragraph on what it gives rewritten below on both reports. Derived in the [pair-block note](../reviews/2026-09-14-I104-pair-block.md) for [I104](../plan.md). The polynomial cores are kernel-checked in [L222Statement.lean](../lean/HamiltonsCubic/Results/L222Statement.lean), and at $P$'s own operators in [L222Op.lean](../lean/HamiltonsCubic/Results/L222Op.lean): `Sjk_mul`, $\mathcal D_n\oplus V_{jk}$ closed under $\circ$, `Sjk_crit`, the criticality equations $Q(D)+Q(\nu)=\theta D$ and $2D\circ\nu=\theta\nu$, `Sjk_mul_weyl`, the same inside $\mathcal W_n$, and `Sjk_pin`, $\langle D,\nu\circ\nu\rangle=\tfrac\theta2\lVert\nu\rVert^2$, whose value $\theta/\sqrt3$ for $\langle D,\hat g\rangle$ is now unconditional (below). Item 3's odd clause is `Sjk_odd` in [L222Swap.lean](../lean/HamiltonsCubic/Results/L222Swap.lean), through `pair_swap_even`, with $\nu\circ D_-=0$ on all of $S_{jk}\cap\mathcal W_n$ as `Sjk_nu_odd_zero` ([r1788](../reviews/REVIEW_r1788.md)), and item 6 is `crit_pair` ([r1789](../reviews/REVIEW_r1789.md)). Item 2's square $\nu_x\circ\nu_x=G_x$ is `Q_nuX` in [PairSquare.lean](../lean/HamiltonsCubic/Results/PairSquare.lean) ([r1800](../reviews/REVIEW_r1800.md), CONFIRMED WITH OBSERVATIONS), and on all of $V_{jk}$ as `Q_pair` through `pair_eq_nuX`, and $V_{jk}$ is exactly the $\nu_x$ with $x$ zero at $j,k$ and $\sum x=0$ by `pair_iff` ([PairConv.lean](../lean/HamiltonsCubic/Results/PairConv.lean), with `nuX_weyl` and `nuX_char`), which types the hand argument behind item 2's *Write $\nu\in V_{jk}$ as $\nu=\sum x_ae_{ja}\odot e_{ka}$*; every Weyl operator of character $\varepsilon_j\varepsilon_k$ being $\nu_c$ for its coefficients ([PairIdent.lean](../lean/HamiltonsCubic/Results/PairIdent.lean), [r1803](../reviews/REVIEW_r1803.md), CONFIRMED WITH OBSERVATIONS). Item 2's norms $\lVert\nu_x\rVert^2=2\lVert x\rVert^2$ and $\lVert G_x\rVert^2=3\lVert x\rVert^4$ are `normSq2_nuX` and `normSq2_Gy` in [PairNorm.lean](../lean/HamiltonsCubic/Results/PairNorm.lean), with $\lVert\nu\circ\nu\rVert^2=\tfrac34\lVert\nu\rVert^4$ on all of $V_{jk}$ as `normSq2_Q_pair`, so item 2 is proved in full and the pinning is unconditional, `Sjk_pin_uncond`: $\langle D,\hat g\rangle=\theta/\sqrt3$ at every critical $W=D+\nu$ with $\nu\ne0$ ([r1804](../reviews/REVIEW_r1804.md), CONFIRMED WITH OBSERVATIONS). Item 4's identity $G_x\circ D^-_y=D^-_z$, $z=\tfrac12\lVert x\rVert^2y+\langle x,y\rangle x$, is `LG_Dminus` in [ItemFour.lean](../lean/HamiltonsCubic/Results/ItemFour.lean) ([r1805](../reviews/REVIEW_r1805.md), CONFIRMED WITH OBSERVATIONS), and item 4 as stated, on every swap-odd Weyl operator of character $1$, is `LG_odd` through the identification both ways, `odd_eq_Dminus` and `Dminus_swap_odd` ([OddPart.lean](../lean/HamiltonsCubic/Results/OddPart.lean), Gate 2 [r1806](../reviews/REVIEW_r1806.md), CONFIRMED WITH OBSERVATIONS; `LG_sum` keeps $\sum y=0$). Item 1's rotation invariance is `Sjk_rot` in [L222Rot.lean](../lean/HamiltonsCubic/Results/L222Rot.lean) ([r1875](../reviews/REVIEW_r1875.md)), and item 5's conclusions at a minimising angle are `L222_min` in [L222Min.lean](../lean/HamiltonsCubic/Results/L222Min.lean). Item 5's infinitesimal form is `xi_D` and `xi_nu` in [L222Xi.lean](../lean/HamiltonsCubic/Results/L222Xi.lean), $\xi\cdot D$ and $\xi\cdot\nu$ as derivatives at $0$ of the rotation $\varphi\mapsto R_\varphi\cdot W$, so every item of this lemma is kernel-checked. This lemma records identities, and the pair-block stratum is argued from them in the [gap note](../reviews/2026-09-14-I104-pair-gap.md).*

**Statement.** *Let $n\ge4$, $j\ne k$, and $\mathcal S_{jk}=\mathcal D_n\oplus V_{jk}$. Write $\nu\in V_{jk}$ as $\nu=\sum_ax_a\,e_{ja}\odot e_{ka}$ ($a\notin\{j,k\}$, $\sum x=0$, $\lVert\nu\rVert^2=2\lVert x\rVert^2$), $D\in\mathcal D_n$ with sectional matrix $K$, and let $\sigma$ swap $j$ and $k$ with $D=D^++D^-$ its even and odd parts, the odd part having entries $K_{ja}=-K_{ka}$. Let $\xi\in\mathfrak{so}(n)$ be the generator with $\xi e_j=e_k$, $\xi e_k=-e_j$. Sums over $a$ run over $a\notin\{j,k\}$.*
1. *$\mathcal S_{jk}$ is a subalgebra, preserved by rotations in the $(j,k)$-plane.*
2. *$\nu\circ\nu=G_x$, the diagonal tensor with $K_{ja}=K_{ka}=x_a^2$, $K_{jk}=-\lVert x\rVert^2$, $K_{ab}=2x_ax_b$, and $\lVert G_x\rVert^2=3\lVert x\rVert^4$, so $\lVert\nu\circ\nu\rVert=\tfrac{\sqrt3}2\lVert\nu\rVert^2$.*
3. *If $W=D+\nu$ is a unit critical point with $\nu\ne0$, then $\langle D,\hat g\rangle=\theta/\sqrt3$ for $\hat g=G_x/\lVert G_x\rVert$, and $2L_{D^+}D^-=\theta D^-$.*
4. *On the odd part, identified with $\{y:\sum y=0\}$, $L_{G_x}$ acts as $y\mapsto\tfrac12\lVert x\rVert^2y+x\langle x,y\rangle$.*
5. *$\xi\cdot D\in V_{jk}$ with coordinates $K_{ja}-K_{ka}$, and $\xi\cdot\nu\in\mathcal D_n$ with $\lVert\xi\cdot\nu\rVert^2=4\lVert\nu\rVert^2$. At an angle of the $(j,k)$-rotation minimising $\lVert\nu\rVert^2$, with $D$, $K$ and $x$ those of the rotated tensor: $\langle x,K_{j\cdot}-K_{k\cdot}\rangle=0$, and $\lVert\nu\rVert^2\le\tfrac12\sum_a(K_{ka}-K_{ja})^2$.*
6. *At a diagonal critical point $D$ and $x\perp\mathbf1$, $\theta\langle D,G_x\rangle=\langle L_{G_x}D,D\rangle$, where $\langle D,G_x\rangle=\sum_{a<b}K_{ab}(G_x)_{ab}$ over unordered pairs.*

**Proof.**
1. The fixed space of $\ker(\varepsilon_j\varepsilon_k)$: a four-set character $\chi_Q$ is $-1$ on the flip at an index of $Q\setminus\{j,k\}$, a pair character $\varepsilon_a\varepsilon_b\ne\varepsilon_j\varepsilon_k$ on the flip at an index of $\{a,b\}\setminus\{j,k\}$. Both witnesses lie in $\ker(\varepsilon_j\varepsilon_k)$, and the fixed space of a group of automorphisms is closed under $\circ$. A rotation in the $(j,k)$-plane commutes with every flip outside $\{j,k\}$ and with $\varepsilon_j\varepsilon_k$.
2. $Q=W^2+W^\#$ with $(R^\#)_{ab,ab}=\sum_{c,c'}[R_{ac,ac'}R_{bc,bc'}-R_{ac,bc'}R_{bc,ac'}]$, which reduces to $(S^2)_{ab}$ on diagonal tensors. For $\nu$: $(\nu^2)_{ja,ja}=(\nu^2)_{ka,ka}=x_a^2$; $(\nu^\#)_{ab,ab}=2x_ax_b$ from $\{c,c'\}=\{j,k\}$; $(\nu^\#)_{jk,jk}=-\sum_ax_a^2$ from the second term at $c=c'=a$; every other diagonal entry vanishes, and the off-diagonal part vanishes by character. The norm over unordered pairs is $2\sum x^4+\lVert x\rVert^4+4\sum_{a<b}x_a^2x_b^2=3\lVert x\rVert^4$. *$G_x$ is [Lemma L122](#lemma-l122-the-pair-block-is-a-graph-laplacian-of-the-sectional-matrix)'s pair block read in reverse, which fixes the sign of $K_{jk}$ a second way, with the zero row sums a third.*
3. $V_{jk}$'s equation is $L_D\nu=\tfrac\theta2\nu$, and $\langle L_D\nu,\nu\rangle=\langle D,\nu\circ\nu\rangle$, so $\langle D,G_x\rangle=\tfrac\theta2\lVert\nu\rVert^2$; divide by $\lVert G_x\rVert=\tfrac{\sqrt3}2\lVert\nu\rVert^2$. The $\mathcal D_n$ equation is $D\circ D+G_x=\theta D$, $G_x$ is swap-even, and $(D\circ D)^-=2D^+\circ D^-$.
4. With the diagonal product $(A\circ B)_{ab}=A_{ab}B_{ab}+\tfrac12\sum_c(A_{ac}B_{cb}+B_{ac}A_{cb})$ and $Y_{ja}=y_a$, $Y_{ka}=-y_a$: $(G\circ Y)_{ja}=x_a^2y_a+\tfrac12\big(\lVert x\rVert^2y_a+2x_a(\langle x,y\rangle-x_ay_a)\big)=\tfrac12\lVert x\rVert^2y_a+x_a\langle x,y\rangle$, and the even part of $G\circ Y$ vanishes.
5. $\Lambda^2\xi$ sends $e_{ja}\mapsto e_{ka}$, $e_{ka}\mapsto-e_{ja}$, and $\xi\cdot W=[\Lambda^2\xi,W]$. For diagonal $D$ the commutator has entries $K_{ja}-K_{ka}$ at $(ja,ka)$ and $(ka,ja)$. For $\nu$ it is diagonal with $\mp2x_a$ at $(ja),(ka)$, norm$^2$ $8\lVert x\rVert^2$. Along $e^{\varphi\xi}$ the $V_{jk}$-part is $\nu+\varphi\,\xi\cdot D+\tfrac{\varphi^2}2\xi^2\cdot\nu+O(\varphi^3)$, so $\lVert\nu(\varphi)\rVert^2=\lVert\nu\rVert^2+2\varphi\langle\nu,\xi\cdot D\rangle+\varphi^2(\lVert\xi\cdot D\rVert^2-\lVert\xi\cdot\nu\rVert^2)+O(\varphi^3)$, and a minimum gives both conditions. Restricting to $a\notin\{j,k\}$ matters: the indices $a=j,k$ would add $2K_{jk}^2$ to the sum. The first condition says $\nu$ is orthogonal to the orbit direction $\xi\cdot D$, which is a $\tfrac\theta2$-eigenvector of $L_D$ at a critical point ([diagonal-half.md](diagonal-half.md)).
6. Pair $Q(D)=\theta D$ with $G_x$ and use total symmetry: $\theta\langle D,G_x\rangle=\langle D\circ D,G_x\rangle=\langle L_{G_x}D,D\rangle$. $\square$

**What it gives.** *With $x\perp y$, where $y$ is the odd part's coordinate vector, the swap parity kills $\langle L_{G_x}D^+,D^-\rangle$ and items 4 and 6 turn a pair-block gap at a diagonal critical point, $\langle D,G_x\rangle\le\theta-2\gamma$ over unit $x\perp\mathbf1$ off the orbit direction for a constant $\gamma>0$, into $\langle L_{G_x}D^+,D^+\rangle+\tfrac12\lVert D^-\rVert^2\le\theta^2-2\gamma\theta$.* **That gap is one ingredient of the pair-block stratum, not the stratum**: the stratum also needs the gap to survive the perturbation the critical equations allow, and constants derived for the chain at large $\lVert\nu\rVert$ ([pair-block note](../reviews/2026-09-14-I104-pair-block.md)). *Over all diagonal critical points the gap is false, [post F48](#post-f48-lambda_maxwidetilde-nlethetam_jk-is-false-at-padded-pairs-and-it-was-never-what-the-pair-clause-asked)'s padded $S^6\times S^6$ refuting it, so the stratum is argued at maximisers in the [gap note](../reviews/2026-09-14-I104-pair-gap.md).* **No screen is cited as evidence for any of it.**

#### Lemma L224, the pair-block subalgebra carries no value above the diagonal: $\sup P=\Theta_n$ on $\mathcal D_n\oplus V_{jk}$, attained only at the $(j,k)$-rotations of L57's maximisers

*Gate 2: [r945](../reviews/REVIEW_r945.md) and [r946](../reviews/REVIEW_r946.md) on identical briefs, every step of the proof CONFIRMED by both, the paragraph on what it gives scoped on r946's report. Derived for [I104](../plan.md). The polynomial cores are kernel-checked in [L224Statement.lean](../lean/HamiltonsCubic/Results/L224Statement.lean), and step 3 at the operators in [L224Cubic.lean](../lean/HamiltonsCubic/Results/L224Cubic.lean): `L224_cubic`, $P(D+\nu)=P(E)+3T(E,D_-,D_-)+3T(E,\nu,\nu)$ with no unit hypothesis, through `Tsym_char_zero`, $T(x,y,z)=0$ when a frame change scales $x,y,z$ by $a,b,c$ with $abc=-1$, and in the record's pairing form $P(D+\nu)=P(E)+3\langle E,D_-\circ D_-\rangle+3\langle E,\nu\circ\nu\rangle$ as `L224_cubic_pair` ([r1795](../reviews/REVIEW_r1795.md), CONFIRMED WITH OBSERVATIONS). Step 2's first square $D^-_y\circ D^-_y=G_y$ is `Q_Dminus` in [DiagSquare.lean](../lean/HamiltonsCubic/Results/DiagSquare.lean), through `Qop2_coordOp` and `Qop2_rank_two` ([r1796](../reviews/REVIEW_r1796.md), CONFIRMED WITH OBSERVATIONS). The second square $\nu_x\circ\nu_x=G_x$ is `Q_nuX` in [PairSquare.lean](../lean/HamiltonsCubic/Results/PairSquare.lean), the $45^\circ$ rotation of the $(j,k)$-plane carrying $D^-_x$ onto $\nu_x$ (`rot45_Dminus`) and fixing $G_x$ (`Gy_rot`). The link from $D_-=\tfrac12(D-\sigma D)$ to the coordinate form is `odd_half_eq_Dminus`, and with it $D_-\circ D_-=G_y$ at every Weyl $D$ of character $1$ is `Q_odd_half` ([OddPart.lean](../lean/HamiltonsCubic/Results/OddPart.lean), [r1806](../reviews/REVIEW_r1806.md), CONFIRMED WITH OBSERVATIONS; both directions as `odd_iff`). Steps 1 and 3 in the record's coordinates are `L224_cubic_G` in [L224Pair.lean](../lean/HamiltonsCubic/Results/L224Pair.lean), $P(D+\nu)=P(E)+3\langle E,G_y\rangle+3\langle E,G_x\rangle$ with $\tfrac12(D-\sigma D)=D^-_y$ and $\nu=\nu_x$, and `L224_norm` in [L224Norm.lean](../lean/HamiltonsCubic/Results/L224Norm.lean), $\lVert D+\nu\rVert^2=\lVert E\rVert^2+2(\lVert y\rVert^2+\lVert x\rVert^2)$ ([r1807](../reviews/REVIEW_r1807.md), CONFIRMED WITH OBSERVATIONS). Step 4 is `L224_step4` in [L224Step4.lean](../lean/HamiltonsCubic/Results/L224Step4.lean): every $D+\nu$ has a Weyl $W'=E+D^-_{\sqrt s\,z_*}$ of character $1$ with $\lVert W'\rVert=\lVert D+\nu\rVert$ and $P(D+\nu)\le P(W')$, $z_*$ a maximiser of $q_E(z)=\langle E,G_z\rangle$ on its unit sphere (`exists_qE_max`), through `cubic_even_odd`, $P(E+D^-_z)=P(E)+3q_E(z)$ (Gate 2 [r1808](../reviews/REVIEW_r1808.md), pending). **The inequality itself is kernel-checked**: `L224_bound`, $P(D+\nu)\le\theta_0(n)\lVert D+\nu\rVert^3$ at $n\ge4$, and `L224_bound_odd`, the odd-$n$ $\Theta_n$ at $n\ge5$, in [L224Bound.lean](../lean/HamiltonsCubic/Results/L224Bound.lean), from `L224_step4` and L57's `L57_diagonal`/`L57_diagonal_odd` through `charOne_diag` ([r1809](../reviews/REVIEW_r1809.md), CONFIRMED WITH OBSERVATIONS). Step 5's first half is `L224_eq_tight` in [L224Eq.lean](../lean/HamiltonsCubic/Results/L224Eq.lean): at $P(W)=\Theta_n\lVert W\rVert^3$ either $y=x=0$, which is `zero_branch` ($D+\nu=E$), or $\lambda=q_E(z_*)>0$ with $q_E(y)=\lambda\lVert y\rVert^2$, $q_E(x)=\lambda\lVert x\rVert^2$ and $W'$ attaining $\Theta_n$, its hypotheses discharged at both parities by `L57_even` and `L57_odd` ([r1812](../reviews/REVIEW_r1812.md), [r1813](../reviews/REVIEW_r1813.md), CONFIRMED WITH OBSERVATIONS; at odd $n$ the $\theta_0$ equality case is empty, $\theta_0(n)>\Theta_n$). L57's equality case on nonzero Weyl operators of character $1$, the bridge step 5 needs, is `charOne_eq_even` and `charOne_eq_odd` in [L57Bridge.lean](../lean/HamiltonsCubic/Results/L57Bridge.lean) (Gate 2 [r1814](../reviews/REVIEW_r1814.md), CONFIRMED WITH OBSERVATIONS: the bridge carries the forcing half of L57's equality clause, a maximiser has the sphere-product form, which is the half step 5 uses). The rank-one pairing is `inner_Zp_Gy` in [L224Rank.lean](../lean/HamiltonsCubic/Results/L224Rank.lean), $\langle tZ'(v),G_z\rangle=t\langle v,z\rangle^2$ at $v_j=-v_k$ for $z$ zero at $j,k$ with $\sum z=0$, which is $q_{B'}(z)=c\langle\bar u,z\rangle^2$, with $G_z$ swap-even by `Gy_swap` and $c=4t/n$ at even $n$ (Gate 2 [r1815](../reviews/REVIEW_r1815.md), CONFIRMED WITH OBSERVATIONS). The line at even $n$ is `L224_line_even` in [L224Line.lean](../lean/HamiltonsCubic/Results/L224Line.lean): at $P(D+\nu)=\theta_0(n)\lVert D+\nu\rVert^3$ either $y=x=0$, or $y$ and $x$ are multiples of one $\bar u\ne0$, deriving $v_j=-v_k$ from $W'$'s nonzero odd part and $\lambda=t\lVert\bar u\rVert^2$ on the way (Gate 2 [r1820](../reviews/REVIEW_r1820.md), pending), and `inner_Zp_Gy_two` gives the pairing at both parities with $c=t(v_j-v_k)^2$. The odd-$n$ line is `L224_line_odd` in [L224LineOdd.lean](../lean/HamiltonsCubic/Results/L224LineOdd.lean), with $\bar u$ the projection of $v$ onto the plane (Gate 2 [r1823](../reviews/REVIEW_r1823.md), CONFIRMED WITH OBSERVATIONS: at both parities the odd part is $\tfrac t2(v_j-v_k)\bar u$, so $W'$'s odd direction is $\bar u$ itself). The finite rotation is in [L224Rot.lean](../lean/HamiltonsCubic/Results/L224Rot.lean): `rot_Dminus` and `rot_nuX` turn $(y,x)$ by the double angle and `rot_even` fixes every swap-even weight (Gate 2 [r1824](../reviews/REVIEW_r1824.md), CONFIRMED WITH OBSERVATIONS: differentiated at $\varphi=0$ they give $\xi\cdot D^-_y=\nu_{2y}$ and $\xi\cdot\nu_x=D^-_{-2x}$ with $\xi e_j=e_k$, no sign flip). **The Statement is proved on the operators**: the inequality `L224_bound`/`L224_bound_odd`, equality forward `L224_eq_even`/`L224_eq_odd` (at $P(D+\nu)=\Theta_n\lVert D+\nu\rVert^3$, $D+\nu=\mathrm{Rot}_{jk}(c,s)\cdot B$ with $B$ of character $1$, $\lVert B\rVert=\lVert D+\nu\rVert$, $P(B)=\Theta_n\lVert B\rVert^3$, of L57's sphere-product form by `charOne_eq_even`/`_odd`) and `L224_eq_converse` (Gate 2 [r1825](../reviews/REVIEW_r1825.md), CONFIRMED WITH OBSERVATIONS: the closure $e^{\varphi\xi}\cdot\mathcal D_n\subseteq S_{jk}$ and $\mathrm{Rot}(c,s)=e^{\varphi\xi}$ hold by reading and are not Lean theorems, needed only for the reading that the equality set is exactly the rotations). Off the Statement's path the Lean owes the link to `pair_value_le` and L57's block-constant form, the link from [L57Block.lean](../lean/HamiltonsCubic/Results/L57Block.lean)'s proved theorems to `pair_value_le`'s hypothesis with L57's equality clause in block-constant form (L57 itself is proved); the inequality of the statement is proved at the operators ([r1809](../reviews/REVIEW_r1809.md) O1). The argument is global: it needs no smallness of $\nu$, no gap and no stability lemma.*

**Statement.** *Let $n\ge4$, $j\ne k$ and $\mathcal S_{jk}=\mathcal D_n\oplus V_{jk}$. Every unit $W\in\mathcal S_{jk}$ has $P(W)\le\Theta_n$. Equality holds exactly when $W=e^{\varphi\xi}\cdot B$ for a unit diagonal $B$ attaining [L57](problem.md)'s equality and an angle $\varphi$, where $\xi$ generates the rotations of the $(j,k)$-plane.*

**Proof.** Indices $a,b$ run over $a\notin\{j,k\}$, and vectors $y,x,z$ live in $\{z\in\mathbb R^{n-2}:\sum z=0\}$. $G_z$ is [L222](#lemma-l222-the-pair-block-calculus-nucirc-nu-the-pinning-the-swap-parity-and-the-rotation-angle-on-mathcal-d_noplus-v_jk)'s diagonal tensor with $K_{ja}=K_{ka}=z_a^2$, $K_{jk}=-\lVert z\rVert^2$, $K_{ab}=2z_az_b$.
1. **Coordinates.** Let $\sigma$ swap $j$ and $k$. It fixes $K_{jk}$ and every $K_{ab}$, so a diagonal $D$ splits as $D=E+D^-_y$ with $E$ swap-even and $D^-_y$ the tensor with $K_{ja}=y_a$, $K_{ka}=-y_a$ and no other entry, which lies in $\mathcal D_n$ exactly when $\sum y=0$, with $\lVert D^-_y\rVert^2=2\lVert y\rVert^2$. Every element of $V_{jk}$ is $\nu_x=\sum_ax_a\,e_{ja}\odot e_{ka}$ with $\lVert\nu_x\rVert^2=2\lVert x\rVert^2$, and $\sigma$ fixes it. So $W=E+D^-_y+\nu_x$ and $\lVert W\rVert^2=\lVert E\rVert^2+2s$ with $s=\lVert y\rVert^2+\lVert x\rVert^2$.
2. **The two squares.** $\nu_x\circ\nu_x=G_x$ is L222's item 2. With the diagonal product $(A\circ A)_{pq}=A_{pq}^2+\sum_cA_{pc}A_{cq}$, $D^-_y\circ D^-_y$ has $(ja)$-entry $y_a^2$, $(jk)$-entry $\sum_ay_a(-y_a)=-\lVert y\rVert^2$ and $(ab)$-entry $y_ay_b+(-y_a)(-y_b)=2y_ay_b$, so it is $G_y$. The entries of $G_z$ are linear in $zz^{\mathsf T}$, so $G_y+G_x=\mathcal G(C)$ for $C=yy^{\mathsf T}+xx^{\mathsf T}$ with $\mathcal G$ linear.
3. **The cubic.** In $P(W)=T(W,W,W)$ a term with an odd number of factors $D^-_y$ vanishes, $\sigma$ being an automorphism that fixes $E$ and $\nu_x$ and reverses $D^-_y$. A term with an odd number of factors $\nu_x$ vanishes by the character $\varepsilon_j\varepsilon_k$ ([L120](#lemma-l120-l_m-is-block-diagonal-over-the-sign-characters-at-every-diagonal-critical-point), L222's item 1). *This is [L219](#lemma-l219-the-reflection-split-the-odd-part-of-a-critical-point-has-zero-cubic-and-is-a-tfractheta2-eigenvector-of-the-even-part)'s reflection split applied at the reflections in $e_j-e_k$ and in $e_j$.* What remains is $P(E)+3T(E,D^-_y,D^-_y)+3T(E,\nu_x,\nu_x)$, and total symmetry with step 2 gives $$P(W)=P(E)+3\langle E,G_y\rangle+3\langle E,G_x\rangle=P(E)+3\langle E,\mathcal G(C)\rangle .$$
4. **Rank one.** Let $\lambda$ be the top eigenvalue of the quadratic form $q_E(z)=\langle E,G_z\rangle$ on $\{\sum z=0\}$ and $z_*$ a unit eigenvector for it. Then $q_E(y)+q_E(x)\le\lambda s=q_E(\sqrt s\,z_*)$, so $W'=E+D^-_{\sqrt sz_*}$ is a unit diagonal tensor with $P(W)\le P(E)+3\lambda s=P(W')\le\Theta_n$ by L57.
5. **Equality.** If $P(W)=\Theta_n$ then $P(W')=\Theta_n$, $q_E(y)=\lambda\lVert y\rVert^2$ and $q_E(x)=\lambda\lVert x\rVert^2$. At $s=0$, $W=E$ is diagonal. At $s>0$, $\lVert E\rVert<1$ gives $P(E)\le\Theta_n\lVert E\rVert^3<\Theta_n$, so $\lambda>0$. $W'$ is an L57 maximiser $B'$ with swap-even part $E$ and nonzero odd part, so $j$ and $k$ lie in different blocks $A\ni j$, $B\ni k$ of $B'$, which is block-constant with values $\alpha_A,\alpha_B$ inside the blocks and $\gamma$ across. Since $G_z$ is swap-even, $q_E=q_{B'}$, and expanding, $q_{B'}(z)=(\alpha_A+\alpha_B-2\gamma)\big(\sum_{a\in A'}z_a\big)^2$ with $A'=A\setminus\{j\}$. On $\{\sum z=0\}$ this is $c\langle\bar u,z\rangle^2$ with $\bar u$ the indicator of $A'$ centred over the $n-2$ coordinates off $\{j,k\}$, which keeps it in the plane $z_j=z_k=0$ (centring over all $n$ leaves it, [r1820](../reviews/REVIEW_r1820.md) O1), so $\lambda=c\lVert\bar u\rVert^2>0$ has the line of $\bar u$ as its whole eigenspace, and both $y$ and $x$ lie on it: $(y,x)=\sqrt s\,\hat u\,(\cos\psi,\sin\psi)$. By L222's item 5, $\xi\cdot E=0$, $\xi\cdot D^-_y=\nu_{2y}$ and $\xi\cdot\nu_x=D^-_{-2x}$, so $e^{\varphi\xi}$ fixes $E$ and rotates $(y,x)$ by $2\varphi$, and $W=e^{\varphi\xi}\cdot W'$ with $2\varphi=\psi$ up to the sign convention of $\xi$. Conversely $P$ is $O(n)$-invariant. $\square$

**What it gives.** **The pair-block stratum of I104 closes for GLOBAL maximisers:** at $n\ge12$ a unit global maximiser of $P$ lying in some $\mathcal S_{jk}$ has value at least $\Theta_n$ by H1, hence exactly $\Theta_n$, and is a rotation of an L57 maximiser, which is a sphere product in another frame. At $4\le n\le11$ the maximum is at least $\sqrt{3/2}>\Theta_n$ (padded $W_{\mathbb{CP}^2}$), so no global maximiser lies in any $\mathcal S_{jk}$. *Local maximisers, which I104's theorem names, are not reached.* *No smallness of $\nu$ enters, so for global maximisers the small-$\nu$ reduction of the [gap note](../reviews/2026-09-14-I104-pair-gap.md) and [L223](problem.md)'s use there are not needed for this stratum.* **The mechanism is that $P$ on $\mathcal S_{jk}$ sees its two swap-odd columns only through their Gram matrix, and a linear function of a positive semidefinite matrix of fixed trace peaks at rank one**, which is a diagonal tensor. *The question it hands to I104 is which larger subalgebras keep that structure, first several pair blocks sharing an index and the four-set blocks they generate.*

#### Lemma L225, the per-block cubic: $\mathop{\mathrm{tr}}\big((M^0)^3\big)\le\frac{k-2}{\sqrt{k(k-1)}}\lvert M^0\rvert^3$ for a trace-free symmetric $k\times k$ matrix, with equality exactly at $M^0=0$ and at a simple positive eigenvalue beside a negative one of multiplicity $k-1$

*Gate 2 passed on one reader, [r947](../reviews/REVIEW_r947.md), every step CONFIRMED, the heading, the attribution and Gate 1's (d) scoped on its report. Derived 2026-09-15 for [I105](../plan.md), the tool [I106](../plan.md) consumes. **A confirmation of [Case–Tyrrell](../references/trace-free-cubic-2023-newton/notes.md)'s bound** ([arXiv:2305.03861](https://arxiv.org/abs/2305.03861), the one-sided norm form of their Proposition 2.2, which they state squared and two-sided at $n\ge3$, $(\mathop{\mathrm{tr}}\mathring A^3)^2\le\frac{(n-2)^2}{n(n-1)}\lvert\mathring A\rvert^6$, with equality at an eigenspace of dimension at least $n-1$ of either sign), proved here by a tangent cubic in place of their $\lambda$-method from sharp Newton. The sign of the equality case and $k=2$ are read off here. **The vector form is the classical bound on sample skewness**, $\lvert g_1\rvert\le\frac{k-2}{\sqrt{k-1}}$ of Wilkins (1944) and Kirby (1974), as cited in [De Michele–De Bartolo](../references/skewness-bounds-2025-arxiv/notes.md), since $g_1=\sqrt k\sum\lambda_i^3$ on the unit trace-free sphere. At $k=3$ it is step 5 of [L178](diagonal-half.md) in other coordinates. The vector statement is kernel-checked in [L225Statement.lean](../lean/HamiltonsCubic/Results/L225Statement.lean).*

**Statement.** *Let $k\ge2$, $c=\sqrt{k(k-1)}$, and $\lambda\in\mathbb R^k$ with $\sum_i\lambda_i=0$ and $\sum_i\lambda_i^2=1$. Then $\sum_i\lambda_i^3\le\frac{k-2}c$, with equality exactly when one entry is $\frac{k-1}c$ and the other $k-1$ entries are $-\frac1c$. Applied to the eigenvalues of a trace-free symmetric $k\times k$ matrix $M^0$ with Frobenius norm $\lvert M^0\rvert$, this reads $\mathop{\mathrm{tr}}\big((M^0)^3\big)\le\frac{k-2}{\sqrt{k(k-1)}}\lvert M^0\rvert^3$, with equality exactly when $M^0=\lvert M^0\rvert\big(\tfrac kc\,uu^{\mathsf T}-\tfrac1cI\big)$ for a unit vector $u$. At $k=3$: $\mathop{\mathrm{tr}}\big((M^0)^3\big)\le\tfrac1{\sqrt6}\lvert M^0\rvert^3$, with equality exactly at nonnegative multiples of $\mathop{\mathrm{diag}}(2,-1,-1)$ in some orthonormal frame.*

*Machine-checked: the vector bound, its equality case and its attainment in [L225Statement.lean](../lean/HamiltonsCubic/Results/L225Statement.lean), and the matrix bound, normalised and unnormalised, with its sharpness and its equality case in eigenvalue form in [L225Matrix.lean](../lean/HamiltonsCubic/Results/L225Matrix.lean). The equality case as a matrix identity, in both normalisations, and the $k=3$ clause in [L225Identity.lean](../lean/HamiltonsCubic/Results/L225Identity.lean).*

**Proof.**
1. **Each entry is bounded.** For fixed $i$, $\sum_{j\ne i}\lambda_j=-\lambda_i$ and $\sum_{j\ne i}\lambda_j^2=1-\lambda_i^2$, so Cauchy–Schwarz over the other $k-1$ entries gives $\lambda_i^2\le(k-1)(1-\lambda_i^2)$, that is $k\lambda_i^2\le k-1$. Hence $\lambda_i\le\sqrt{(k-1)/k}=\frac{k-1}c=:\alpha$.
2. **The tangent cubic.** Put $\beta=\frac1c$. For $x\le\alpha$, $(x-\alpha)(x+\beta)^2\le0$. Summing over the entries, $$0\ge\sum_i(\lambda_i-\alpha)(\lambda_i+\beta)^2=\sum_i\lambda_i^3+(2\beta-\alpha)\sum_i\lambda_i^2+(\beta^2-2\alpha\beta)\sum_i\lambda_i-k\alpha\beta^2 .$$ With $\sum\lambda=0$, $\sum\lambda^2=1$ and $k\alpha\beta^2=\frac{k(k-1)}{c^3}=\frac1c=\beta$, this is $\sum_i\lambda_i^3\le\alpha-\beta=\frac{k-2}c$.
3. **Equality.** At equality the sum in step 2 is zero and each of its terms is $\le0$, so each term vanishes and every $\lambda_i$ is $\alpha$ or $-\beta$. With $m$ entries at $\alpha$, $\sum\lambda=0$ reads $m(k-1)-(k-m)=0$, so $m=1$. Conversely $(\alpha,-\beta,\dots,-\beta)$ has $\sum\lambda=\frac{(k-1)-(k-1)}c=0$, $\sum\lambda^2=\frac{(k-1)^2+(k-1)}{c^2}=1$ and $\sum\lambda^3=\frac{(k-1)^3-(k-1)}{c^3}=\frac{k(k-1)(k-2)}{c^3}=\frac{k-2}c$.
4. **Matrices.** In an orthonormal eigenframe of $M^0$, $\mathop{\mathrm{tr}}M^0=\sum\lambda_i$, $\lvert M^0\rvert^2=\sum\lambda_i^2$ and $\mathop{\mathrm{tr}}\big((M^0)^3\big)=\sum\lambda_i^3$, so dividing by $\lvert M^0\rvert$ gives the matrix form, the case $M^0=0$ being trivial. The equality spectrum $(\alpha,-\beta,\dots,-\beta)$ is that of $\tfrac kc\,uu^{\mathsf T}-\tfrac1cI$ with $u$ the eigenvector of $\alpha$. $\square$

**The case $k=3$ as one identity.** With $\lambda=(a,b,-a-b)$, $\sum\lambda^2=2(a^2+ab+b^2)$ and $\sum\lambda^3=-3ab(a+b)$, and $$\Big(\sum\lambda^2\Big)^3-6\Big(\sum\lambda^3\Big)^2=2(a-b)^2(2a+b)^2(a+2b)^2\ge0,$$ which is the two-sided bound $\lvert\sum\lambda^3\rvert\le\tfrac1{\sqrt6}\big(\sum\lambda^2\big)^{3/2}$ squared, with zero set the three lines on which two entries agree, the negative spectrum $(-2,1,1)/\sqrt6$ included. *The right side is twice the discriminant $\prod_{i<j}(\lambda_i-\lambda_j)^2$, so this is [L178](diagonal-half.md)'s step 5, $\tfrac12-27e_3^2\ge0$ at $\sum\lambda^2=1$, written as a square.*

**Gate 1.** (a) At $k=3$ the constant $\tfrac1{\sqrt6}$ and the equality spectrum $(2,-1,-1)/\sqrt6$ reproduce [L178](diagonal-half.md)'s step 5 and Case–Tyrrell's reading at $n=3$. (b) The $k=3$ bound is reached a second way by the identity above, which shares no step with the tangent cubic. (c) `cube_sum_attained` exhibits the equality vector at every $k\ge2$, and the file carries a witness at $k=2$. (d) At $k=2$ the bound is $0$, met identically on $(\lambda,-\lambda)$, which is the vanishing of $\mathop{\mathrm{tr}}\big((M^0)^3\big)$ on $2\times2$ blocks that the [block note](../reviews/2026-09-14-I104-block-J.md) reads as the reason the value in [L224](#lemma-l224-the-pair-block-subalgebra-carries-no-value-above-the-diagonal-sup-ptheta_n-on-mathcal-d_noplus-v_jk-attained-only-at-the-jk-rotations-of-l57s-maximisers) is quadratic, where L224's own step 3 reaches it by parity.

**Lean.** `entry_sq_le` is step 1, `cube_sum_le` step 2, `cube_sum_eq` and `cube_sum_eq_card` the forward half of step 3, `cube_sum_attained` its converse, and `three_by_three` the $k=3$ identity. Step 4 is `eq_identity` through `sym_spectral_gen`, Mathlib's spectral theorem, in [L225Identity.lean](../lean/HamiltonsCubic/Results/L225Identity.lean).

**What it gives.** **A sharp bound on each block's cubic $\mathop{\mathrm{tr}}\big((M_a^0)^3\big)$ on $\mathcal S_J$, one block at a time, rigid at $k\ge3$.** *It relates no two frames: how the eigenframes of different $M_a$ and of $R_J$ sit against each other is the whole of [I106](../plan.md)'s remaining question, and the plan's abandon condition for [R27](../routes.md) is a bound assembled from L225 per block against separately bounded couplings, the decoupled shape [F67](fence-posts.md) fences.*

#### Lemma L226, the cubic on the block subalgebra $\mathcal S_J$: every coupling carries the coefficient $3$, and at $\lvert J\rvert=3$ Ricci-flatness fixes $R_J$ as $N-\tfrac12(\mathop{\mathrm{tr}}N)I$

*Gate 2 passed: [r948](../reviews/REVIEW_r948.md) and [r949](../reviews/REVIEW_r949.md) on identical briefs, every step CONFIRMED by both, with the definition of $\mathcal S_J$, three links of the chain and the scope of Gate 1 and of the Lean file repaired on their reports. Derived 2026-09-15 for [I106](../plan.md), the coefficients the [2026-09-14c sweep](../reviews/2026-09-14c-LIT-step8-sweep.md) wrote unverified, in the coordinates of the [block note](../reviews/2026-09-14-I104-block-J.md). The operator and the bridge to $P$ are typed in [L226Statement.lean](../lean/HamiltonsCubic/Results/L226Statement.lean), and both items are kernel-checked at every $k$ on the block form: item 2 as `isWeyl_blockOpG_iff` in [L226Gen.lean](../lean/HamiltonsCubic/Results/L226Gen.lean) and item 1 as `Pcubic_blockOpG` in [L226GenC.lean](../lean/HamiltonsCubic/Results/L226GenC.lean), with $\mathop{\mathrm{tr}}(R_J\Lambda^2M)$ identified by `trRL2_eq`.*

**Setting.** Fix $J\subseteq[n]$ with $\lvert J\rvert=k\ge2$, outer set $A=[n]\setminus J$ and $m=n-k$. Put $V_a=\mathop{\mathrm{span}}\{\varphi_{pa}:p\in J\}$, identified with $\mathbb R^J$ by $x\mapsto\sum_px_p\varphi_{pa}$, and for $x,y\in\mathbb R^J$ put $x\wedge y=\sum_{p,q}x_py_q\varphi_{pq}\in\Lambda^2\mathbb R^J$. Here $\mathcal S_J$ is the fixed space of $H_J$ in the symmetric operators on $\Lambda^2\mathbb R^n$. The block note's character argument uses no Weyl condition and gives the same block form on it, and the block note's $\mathcal S_J$ is $\mathcal S_J\cap\mathcal W_n$ in this notation. By the block note, $W\in\mathcal S_J$ is block-diagonal on $\Lambda^2\mathbb R^n=\Lambda^2\mathbb R^J\oplus\bigoplus_{a\in A}V_a\oplus\Lambda^2\mathbb R^A$: a symmetric $R_J$ on $\Lambda^2\mathbb R^J$, a symmetric $M_a$ on $V_a$ with $(M_a)_{pq}=W_{pa,qa}$, and $W\varphi_{ab}=K_{ab}\varphi_{ab}$. So $\lVert W\rVert^2=\lvert R_J\rvert^2+\sum_a\lvert M_a\rvert^2+\sum_{a<b}K_{ab}^2$. $\Lambda^2M$ is the operator $x\wedge y\mapsto Mx\wedge My$ on $\Lambda^2\mathbb R^J$.

**Statement.**
1. *For every $W\in\mathcal S_J$,* $$P(W)=P(R_J)+\sum_{a\in A}\Big(\mathop{\mathrm{tr}}M_a^3+3\mathop{\mathrm{tr}}\big(R_J\,\Lambda^2M_a\big)\Big)+\sum_{a<b}\Big(K_{ab}^3+3K_{ab}\mathop{\mathrm{tr}}(M_aM_b)\Big)+3\sum_{a<b<c}K_{ab}K_{ac}K_{bc},$$ *where $P(R_J)=\mathop{\mathrm{tr}}R_J^3+\langle R_J,R_J^\#\rangle$ is the cubic of $\Lambda^2\mathbb R^J=\mathfrak{so}(k)$.*
2. *$W\in\mathcal S_J$ lies in $\mathcal W_n$ exactly when $R_J$ satisfies the first Bianchi identity on $\mathbb R^J$, $\rho(R_J)+\sum_aM_a=0$ with $\rho(R_J)_{pq}=\sum_{r\in J}\langle R_J\varphi_{pr},\varphi_{qr}\rangle$, and $\mathop{\mathrm{tr}}M_a+\sum_{b\ne a}K_{ab}=0$ at every $a\in A$.*
3. *At $k=3$, write $R_J$ in the orthonormal frame $\varphi_{23},\varphi_{31},\varphi_{12}$ as a symmetric $S$. Then $P(R_J)=\mathop{\mathrm{tr}}S^3+3\det S$, $\mathop{\mathrm{tr}}(R_J\Lambda^2M)=\mathop{\mathrm{tr}}(S\mathop{\mathrm{adj}}M)$, $\rho(R_J)=(\mathop{\mathrm{tr}}S)I-S$, and Bianchi holds automatically. So on $\mathcal S_J\cap\mathcal W_n$, with $N=\sum_aM_a$, $S=N-\tfrac12(\mathop{\mathrm{tr}}N)I$ and* $$P(W)=\mathop{\mathrm{tr}}S^3+3\det S+\sum_a\Big(\mathop{\mathrm{tr}}M_a^3+3\mathop{\mathrm{tr}}(S\mathop{\mathrm{adj}}M_a)\Big)+\sum_{a<b}\Big(K_{ab}^3+3K_{ab}\mathop{\mathrm{tr}}(M_aM_b)\Big)+3\sum_{a<b<c}K_{ab}K_{ac}K_{bc}.$$

**Proof.**
1. **$P$ in an eigenbasis.** Let $v_\alpha$ be an orthonormal eigenbasis of $W$ with eigenvalues $\lambda_\alpha$, and $c_{\alpha\beta\gamma}=\langle[v_\alpha,v_\beta],v_\gamma\rangle$, totally antisymmetric. The index formula of [conventions.md](conventions.md), $(W^\#)_{\alpha\beta}=\tfrac12\sum c_{\alpha\gamma\delta}c_{\beta\epsilon\zeta}W_{\gamma\epsilon}W_{\delta\zeta}$, gives $\langle W,W^\#\rangle=\tfrac12\sum_{\alpha,\beta,\gamma}\lambda_\alpha\lambda_\beta\lambda_\gamma c_{\alpha\beta\gamma}^2$ over ordered triples, and $\mathop{\mathrm{tr}}W^3=\sum\lambda_\alpha^3$. *At $W=\mathrm{id}$ the first is $\tfrac12\cdot2(n-2)\dim\Lambda^2$, which is $\mathrm{id}^\#=(n-2)\mathrm{id}$.* $W\in\mathcal S_J$ is block-diagonal, so the eigenbasis is chosen block by block, taking $\varphi_{ab}$ itself as the eigenvector on each line $\mathbb R\varphi_{ab}$ of $\Lambda^2\mathbb R^A$.
2. **Brackets between blocks.** From $[\varphi_{ab},\varphi_{cd}]=\delta_{ac}\varphi_{bd}+\delta_{bd}\varphi_{ac}-\delta_{ad}\varphi_{bc}-\delta_{bc}\varphi_{ad}$, for distinct $p,q\in J$ and distinct $a,b,c\in A$: $[\varphi_{pa},\varphi_{qa}]=\varphi_{pq}$, $[\varphi_{pa},\varphi_{pb}]=\varphi_{ab}$, $[\varphi_{pa},\varphi_{qb}]=0$, $[\varphi_{pa},\varphi_{ab}]=-\varphi_{pb}$, $[\varphi_{pa},\varphi_{bc}]=0$, $[\varphi_{ab},\varphi_{ac}]=\varphi_{bc}$, $[\varphi_{pq},\varphi_{ra}]=\delta_{pr}\varphi_{qa}-\delta_{qr}\varphi_{pa}$, $[\varphi_{pq},\varphi_{rs}]\in\Lambda^2\mathbb R^J$, and $\Lambda^2\mathbb R^J$ commutes with $\Lambda^2\mathbb R^A$. So $[V_a,V_a]\subseteq\Lambda^2\mathbb R^J$ with $[x,y]=x\wedge y$, $[V_a,V_b]=\mathbb R\varphi_{ab}$ with $[x,y]=\langle x,y\rangle\varphi_{ab}$, $[\Lambda^2\mathbb R^J,V_a]\subseteq V_a$, $[V_a,\varphi_{ab}]\subseteq V_b$, and $\Lambda^2\mathbb R^J$ and $\Lambda^2\mathbb R^A$ are subalgebras. Hence $c_{\alpha\beta\gamma}\ne0$ only when the three blocks are, up to order, three copies of $\Lambda^2\mathbb R^J$, one of $\Lambda^2\mathbb R^J$ with two of one $V_a$, the three blocks $V_a,V_b,\mathbb R\varphi_{ab}$, or three pairs of $A$ forming a triangle.
3. **The four sums.** *Three copies of $\Lambda^2\mathbb R^J$*: the structure constants are those of $\mathfrak{so}(k)$, so these triples give $\langle R_J,R_J^\#\rangle$ and, with $\mathop{\mathrm{tr}}R_J^3$, $P(R_J)$. *$\Lambda^2\mathbb R^J$ with two of $V_a$*: the one $\Lambda^2\mathbb R^J$ vector takes $3$ positions in an ordered triple. For orthonormal eigenvectors $v_\beta,v_\gamma$ of $M_a$, $v_\beta\wedge v_\gamma$ is an eigenvector of $\Lambda^2M_a$ with eigenvalue $\lambda_\beta\lambda_\gamma$, and since $\langle x\wedge y,u\wedge v\rangle=\langle x,u\rangle\langle y,v\rangle-\langle x,v\rangle\langle y,u\rangle$ these form an orthonormal basis of $\Lambda^2\mathbb R^J$ over $\beta<\gamma$, so for a unit eigenvector $\omega$ of $R_J$ with eigenvalue $\mu$, $\sum_{\beta,\gamma}\lambda_\beta\lambda_\gamma\langle v_\beta\wedge v_\gamma,\omega\rangle^2=2\langle\Lambda^2M_a\,\omega,\omega\rangle$. Summing, $\tfrac12\cdot3\cdot2\sum_\omega\mu\langle\Lambda^2M_a\omega,\omega\rangle=3\mathop{\mathrm{tr}}(R_J\Lambda^2M_a)$. *$V_a,V_b,\mathbb R\varphi_{ab}$*: $6$ orders, and $\sum_{\beta,\gamma}\lambda_\beta(M_a)\lambda_\gamma(M_b)\langle v_\beta,v_\gamma\rangle^2=\mathop{\mathrm{tr}}(M_aM_b)$, giving $\tfrac12\cdot6\,K_{ab}\mathop{\mathrm{tr}}(M_aM_b)$. *Triangles in $A$*: $6$ orders and $c^2=1$, giving $3K_{ab}K_{ac}K_{bc}$. With $\mathop{\mathrm{tr}}W^3=\mathop{\mathrm{tr}}R_J^3+\sum_a\mathop{\mathrm{tr}}M_a^3+\sum_{a<b}K_{ab}^3$ this is item 1.
4. **The Weyl conditions.** $W$ is symmetric and preserves $\Lambda^2$ by construction. An entry $W_{ij,kl}$ can be nonzero only when its index multiset lies in $J$, is $\{p,q,a,a\}$, or is $\{a,b,a,b\}$. A Bianchi sum with a repeated index follows from the pair symmetries, and one over four distinct indices not all in $J$ meets only entries of none of these shapes, so Bianchi reduces to $R_J$'s. For Ricci, $\mathop{\mathrm{Ric}}_{pq}=\rho(R_J)_{pq}+\sum_a(M_a)_{pq}$ and $\mathop{\mathrm{Ric}}_{aa}=\mathop{\mathrm{tr}}M_a+\sum_{b\ne a}K_{ab}$, while $\mathop{\mathrm{Ric}}_{pa}$ and $\mathop{\mathrm{Ric}}_{ab}$ pair vectors of different blocks and vanish. This is item 2.
5. **$k=3$.** The frame $\varphi_{23},\varphi_{31},\varphi_{12}\mapsto e_1,e_2,e_3$ carries $x\wedge y$ to $x\times y$, and it carries the bracket of $\Lambda^2\mathbb R^J$ to the cross product, since the bracket formula gives $[\varphi_{23},\varphi_{31}]=\varphi_{12}$ and its cyclic images. So every distinct orthonormal triple has $c^2=1$, and $\langle R_J,R_J^\#\rangle=\tfrac12\cdot6\det S$. The cofactor identity $Mx\times My=\mathop{\mathrm{adj}}(M)(x\times y)$ for symmetric $M$ makes $\Lambda^2M$ the adjugate. Entry by entry, $\rho_{11}=S_{22}+S_{33}$ and $\rho_{12}=-S_{12}$, so $\rho(S)=(\mathop{\mathrm{tr}}S)I-S$. Every symmetric operator on $\Lambda^2\mathbb R^3$ satisfies Bianchi, since four indices from a set of three repeat one and step 4's repeated-index case applies. Taking traces in $(\mathop{\mathrm{tr}}S)I-S=-N$ gives $\mathop{\mathrm{tr}}S=-\tfrac12\mathop{\mathrm{tr}}N$, hence $S=N-\tfrac12(\mathop{\mathrm{tr}}N)I$. $\square$

**Gate 1.** (a) *A trusted value by the same formula.* At $W=\mathrm{id}$ and $k=3$, not Weyl, item 1 reads $6+12m+5m(m-1)+\tfrac12m(m-1)(m-2)$, which is $\tfrac12(m+3)(m+2)^2=(n-1)\dim\Lambda^2=P(\mathrm{id})$, checked at $m=1,2,3$ as $18,40,75$ and identical as polynomials in $m$. (b) *A second route.* At a coordinate-diagonal $W$, item 1 at $k=3$ written with item 3's first two identities, $S$ free, becomes $\sum_{i<j}K_{ij}^3+3\sum_{i<j<l}K_{ij}K_{il}K_{jl}$, the diagonal form $\mathop{\mathrm{tr}}W^3+\langle W,W^\#\rangle=\tfrac12\langle K^{\circ2},K\rangle+\tfrac12\langle K^2,K\rangle$, each triangle type $JJJ$, $JJA$, $JAA$, $AAA$ arising once. (c) *A screen.* [screen_block_SJ.py](../scripts/misc/screen_block_SJ.py) builds a random block operator and a random Weyl element of $\mathcal S_J$ at $n=6,7$, and item 3 agrees with the trusted $Q$ of [curvature_operator.py](../scripts/lib/curvature_operator.py) to twelve digits, the Ricci contraction of the Weyl element vanishes to $4\cdot10^{-16}$, $\rho(S)=(\mathop{\mathrm{tr}}S)I-S$ holds to $6\cdot10^{-17}$, and replacing the cofactor coefficient $3$ by $2$ moves $P$ by $0.8$ and $2.5$. It is a screen of a derived formula and establishes nothing by itself. (d) *At $k=2$*, $\Lambda^2\mathbb R^J$ is one line, $\rho(R_J)=K_{jk}I$ and item 2 reads $\sum_aM_a=-K_{jk}I$, whose traceless part is [L224](#lemma-l224-the-pair-block-subalgebra-carries-no-value-above-the-diagonal-sup-ptheta_n-on-mathcal-d_noplus-v_jk-attained-only-at-the-jk-rotations-of-l57s-maximisers)'s $\sum y=\sum x=0$.

**Lean.** [L226Statement.lean](../lean/HamiltonsCubic/Results/L226Statement.lean) builds the operator of item 3 as `blockOp` from `blockCoeff`, whose entries [screen_blockcoeff_L226.py](../scripts/misc/screen_blockcoeff_L226.py) matched to the operator of (c) at $n=6,7$ with zero difference. `Pcubic_blockOp` states item 1 at $k=3$ in item 3's coordinates with $S$ free, `normSq_blockOp` the Setting's norm at $k=3$, and `Ric_blockOp_J` and `Ric_blockOp_outer` the diagonal Ricci entries of item 2 at $k=3$, all four proved against the base layer. [L226Mixed.lean](../lean/HamiltonsCubic/Results/L226Mixed.lean) proves the mixed entries (`Ric_blockOp_Jout`, `Ric_blockOp_outJ`, `Ric_blockOp_outer_ne`), item 2's Ricci conditions at $\lvert J\rvert=3$ (`ricciFlat_iff`) and item 3's closing formula at a Ricci-flat block operator (`Pcubic_ricciFlat`). It also proves that `blockOp` preserves skew matrices and is self-adjoint (`blockOp_isSkew`, `blockOp_selfAdjoint`, on all matrices), satisfies the first Bianchi identity (`blockOp_bianchi`, item 3's automatic Bianchi), and lies in $\mathcal W_n$ exactly when item 2's Ricci conditions hold (`isWeyl_blockOp_iff`, item 2 at $\lvert J\rvert=3$). At every $k$, [L226Gen.lean](../lean/HamiltonsCubic/Results/L226Gen.lean) proves item 2 as `isWeyl_blockOpG_iff` and [L226GenA.lean](../lean/HamiltonsCubic/Results/L226GenA.lean), [L226GenB.lean](../lean/HamiltonsCubic/Results/L226GenB.lean) and [L226GenC.lean](../lean/HamiltonsCubic/Results/L226GenC.lean) prove item 1 as `Pcubic_blockOpG`, with $R_J$ an array carrying the curvature symmetries, $M_a$ and $K$ symmetric; the block form of $W\in\mathcal S_J$ is the Setting's input and is not derived in Lean ([r1890](../reviews/REVIEW_r1890.md)). `ricciJ_inverse` (step 5's inversion), `cubicJ_diagonal` and `coupling_diagonal` (Gate 1's (b) for the $J$ terms) and `blockP_identity` (Gate 1's (a) at $m=3$) are proved.

**What it gives.** **[I106](../plan.md)'s objective, explicit.** *On the unit sphere of $\mathcal S_J\cap\mathcal W_n$ at $\lvert J\rvert=3$, $P$ is a cubic in $m$ symmetric $3\times3$ blocks and the outer diagonal, $R_J$ eliminated. The blocks meet the frames of the others in two ways: through $S=N-\tfrac12(\mathop{\mathrm{tr}}N)I$, which enters $\mathop{\mathrm{tr}}S^3+3\det S$, every $3\mathop{\mathrm{tr}}(S\mathop{\mathrm{adj}}M_a)$ and the norm $\lvert S\rvert^2$, and through $3K_{ab}\mathop{\mathrm{tr}}(M_aM_b)$.* Each block's own cubic is bounded by [L225](second-order.md).

#### Lemma L227, the $\lvert J\rvert=3$ step of R27 at $n=5$: $\sup P=\Theta_5$ on $\mathcal S_J\cap\mathcal W_5$, attained at commuting blocks

*Gate 2 passed: [r950](../reviews/REVIEW_r950.md) and [r951](../reviews/REVIEW_r951.md) on identical briefs, every step CONFIRMED by both, with Gate 1, step 4's citations and the closing paragraph repaired on their reports. Derived 2026-09-15 after the user reported switching to Fable, for [I106](../plan.md) ([derivation](../reviews/2026-09-15-I106-derivation.md), batch 4), and recorded on Opus with the alignment written as two Rayleigh bounds. Kernel-checked with no `sorry` in [L227Statement.lean](../lean/HamiltonsCubic/Results/L227Statement.lean) and [L227Alignment.lean](../lean/HamiltonsCubic/Results/L227Alignment.lean).
