L227 inherits the two identities L57's block names as carrying L57: the vertex-matrix form of the cubic, which [L226](second-order.md)'s Gate 1 (b) reads at a coordinate-diagonal $W$, and the norm formula for $Z(v)$, which [L223](problem.md)'s block reports deriving.*

**Statement.** *Let $J\subseteq[5]$ with $\lvert J\rvert=3$ and $\mathcal S_J$ as in [L226](second-order.md). Every unit $W\in\mathcal S_J\cap\mathcal W_5$ has $P(W)\le\Theta_5=\tfrac{2\sqrt2}3$, and the value $\Theta_5$ is attained at a unit $W$ whose blocks $M_a$ and $S$ commute, a coordinate-diagonal tensor in a frame of $\mathbb R^5$ rotated inside $\mathbb R^J$.*

**Proof.** Write the outer set as $\{a,b\}$ and use L226's item 3.
1. **The objective at two outer indices.** L226's item 2 gives $\mathop{\mathrm{tr}}M_a=\mathop{\mathrm{tr}}M_b=-K_{ab}$, so $M_a=mI+X_1$, $M_b=mI+X_2$ with $\mathop{\mathrm{tr}}X_i=0$ and $K_{ab}=-3m$, the scalar $m=\tfrac13\mathop{\mathrm{tr}}M_a$ being distinct from L226's $m=n-k$, which is $2$ here, and $S=N-\tfrac12(\mathop{\mathrm{tr}}N)I=X_1+X_2-mI$. For a traceless $3\times3$ $X$, Cayley–Hamilton gives $\mathop{\mathrm{adj}}(mI+X)=X^2-mX+\big(m^2-\tfrac12\mathop{\mathrm{tr}}X^2\big)I$ and $\det(X-mI)=\tfrac13\mathop{\mathrm{tr}}X^3+\tfrac12m\mathop{\mathrm{tr}}X^2-m^3$. Substituting,
$$P(W)=6\big(\mathop{\mathrm{tr}}X_1^3+\mathop{\mathrm{tr}}X_2^3\big)+9\big(\mathop{\mathrm{tr}}X_1^2X_2+\mathop{\mathrm{tr}}X_1X_2^2\big)-18m\mathop{\mathrm{tr}}(X_1X_2)-72m^3,$$
$$\lVert W\rVert^2=2\big(\lvert X_1\rvert^2+\lvert X_2\rvert^2\big)+2\mathop{\mathrm{tr}}(X_1X_2)+18m^2 .$$
2. **The change of variables.** Put $U=X_1+X_2$ and $V=X_1-X_2$, traceless and symmetric. By cyclicity of the trace, $\mathop{\mathrm{tr}}X_1^3+\mathop{\mathrm{tr}}X_2^3=\tfrac14\mathop{\mathrm{tr}}U^3+\tfrac34\mathop{\mathrm{tr}}(UV^2)$, $\mathop{\mathrm{tr}}X_1^2X_2+\mathop{\mathrm{tr}}X_1X_2^2=\mathop{\mathrm{tr}}(UX_1X_2)=\tfrac14\mathop{\mathrm{tr}}U^3-\tfrac14\mathop{\mathrm{tr}}(UV^2)$ and $\mathop{\mathrm{tr}}(X_1X_2)=\tfrac14\big(\lvert U\rvert^2-\lvert V\rvert^2\big)$, so
$$P(W)=\tfrac{15}4\mathop{\mathrm{tr}}U^3+\tfrac94\mathop{\mathrm{tr}}(UV^2)-\tfrac92m\lvert U\rvert^2+\tfrac92m\lvert V\rvert^2-72m^3,\qquad\lVert W\rVert^2=\tfrac32\lvert U\rvert^2+\tfrac12\lvert V\rvert^2+18m^2 .$$
**At fixed $m$, $U$ and $\lvert V\rvert$, the frame of $V$ enters only through $\mathop{\mathrm{tr}}(UV^2)$, and the norm not at all.**
3. **The alignment.** Take an orthonormal eigenframe $Q\in SO(3)$ of $U$ with eigenvalues $u_1\ge u_2\ge u_3$, let $d_i=(Q^{\mathsf T}V^2Q)_{ii}$, and let $w_1\ge w_2\ge w_3\ge0$ be the eigenvalues of $V^2$, the squares of $V$'s eigenvalues $\nu_i$ indexed so that $\nu_i^2=w_i$. By Rayleigh, $d_1\le w_1$ and $d_3\ge w_3$, and $\sum d_i=\mathop{\mathrm{tr}}V^2=\sum w_i$, so $d_1+d_2\le w_1+w_2$. Then
$$\mathop{\mathrm{tr}}(UV^2)=\sum_iu_id_i=u_3\sum_id_i+(u_2-u_3)(d_1+d_2)+(u_1-u_2)d_1\le\sum_iu_iw_i .$$
The matrix $V'=Q\mathop{\mathrm{diag}}(\nu_1,\nu_2,\nu_3)Q^{\mathsf T}$ is traceless and symmetric, commutes with $U$, has $\lvert V'\rvert=\lvert V\rvert$ and $\mathop{\mathrm{tr}}(UV'^2)=\sum_iu_iw_i\ge\mathop{\mathrm{tr}}(UV^2)$.
4. **The comparison tensor.** $X_1'=\tfrac12(U+V')$ and $X_2'=\tfrac12(U-V')$ are traceless, symmetric and diagonal in $Q$, and with the same $m$ and $K$, and the same $S=X_1'+X_2'-mI=U-mI$, they define a block operator $W'$, which lies in $\mathcal S_J$ because every block entry carries a character trivial on $H_J$ ([block note](../reviews/2026-09-14-I104-block-J.md)) and in $\mathcal W_5$ by L226's items 2 and 3, with $\lVert W'\rVert=\lVert W\rVert$ and $P(W')-P(W)=\tfrac94\big(\mathop{\mathrm{tr}}(UV'^2)-\mathop{\mathrm{tr}}(UV^2)\big)\ge0$ by step 2. The rotation of $\mathbb R^J$ by $Q^{\mathsf T}$ conjugates every $M_a$ and, since $Q\in SO(3)$ acts on the frame $\varphi_{23},\varphi_{31},\varphi_{12}$ by itself, conjugates $S$ as well (any orthogonal $Q$ would do, since $\Lambda^2Q$ acts on that frame by $(\det Q)\,Q$), so it carries $W'$ to a tensor whose blocks and $S$ are diagonal. That tensor is the coordinate-diagonal Weyl operator of a vertex matrix in $\mathcal D_5$, by L57's domain bijection, of unit norm, and it has the value of $W'$, since $P$, the norm and $\mathcal W_5$ are invariant under $O(5)$. [L57](problem.md) gives $P(W)\le P(W')\le\Theta_5$. **Attainment**: $\mathcal D_5\subseteq\mathcal S_J$, and L57's maximiser, the vertex tensor of $S^2\times S^3$, has value $\Theta_5$. $\square$

**Gate 1.** (a) *A trusted value.* Step 1's polynomial and step 2's form in $U,V$ agree with the trusted $Q$ of [curvature_operator.py](../scripts/lib/curvature_operator.py) at $n=5$ to ten digits, and both norm formulas agree with L226's block norm to ten digits ([screen_I106.py](../scripts/misc/screen_I106.py)). The norm is compared formula to formula, not against the trusted operator. (b) *A second route.* `blockP_m2` specialises [L226Statement.lean](../lean/HamiltonsCubic/Results/L226Statement.lean)'s `blockP` to step 1 in the kernel, independently of the Cayley–Hamilton expansions written here, and `value_UV`, `norm_UV` check step 2. (c) *A screen.* A bounded ascent from three random starts on $\mathcal S_J\cap\mathcal W_5$ reached $0.942809=\Theta_5$ at blocks commuting to $2\cdot10^{-8}$, the $S^2\times S^3$ tensor. It is a screen and establishes nothing. (d) *At one outer index* L226's item 3, with $\mathop{\mathrm{tr}}M_a=0$ from item 2 and no $K$, reads $P=6\mathop{\mathrm{tr}}X_1^3$, which is step 1's formula at $X_2=0$ and $m=0$, at $\lVert W\rVert^2=2\lvert X_1\rvert^2$, and [L225](second-order.md) gives $\Theta_4=\tfrac{\sqrt3}2$, the $n=4$ case.

**Lean.** In [L227Statement.lean](../lean/HamiltonsCubic/Results/L227Statement.lean), `blockP_m2` and `normSq_m2` are step 1 against L226's polynomial and norm, `value_UV` and `norm_UV` step 2, `rearrangement` step 3's inequality, and `alignment_step` the existence of $V'$ in step 3, stated with commuting with $U$ in place of diagonal in $Q$ and without the isospectral clause, which its consumer does not need. `alignment_step` is proved in [L227Alignment.lean](../lean/HamiltonsCubic/Results/L227Alignment.lean), at every size and by a second route: the squared entries of the orthogonal matrix joining the eigenframes of $U$ and $V$ are doubly stochastic, so by Birkhoff's theorem $\mathop{\mathrm{tr}}(UV^2)$ is at most its value at a permutation, and that permutation gives $V'$. `commuting_improvement`, steps 3 and 4 up to the membership of $W'$ and the rotation, stated for the polynomials `valueX` and `normX`, follows. **No declaration uses `sorry`.** The rotation to a coordinate-diagonal tensor and L57's use are proved at the operator for any diagonal constant $c$: `L57_block_of` in [L57Block.lean](../lean/HamiltonsCubic/Results/L57Block.lean) (a Ricci-flat `blockOp S M K` with $S$ and every $M_a$ diagonal has $P(W)\le c\lVert W\rVert^3$, $n=m+3\ge4$, whenever $c$ bounds the coordinate-diagonal case) and `L57_block_conj_of` in [L57BlockConj.lean](../lean/HamiltonsCubic/Results/L57BlockConj.lean) (the same when one orthogonal $F$ diagonalises $S$ and every $M_a$ at once), with instances `L57_block`, `L57_block_conj` at $c=\theta_0(n)$ for even $n$ and `L57_block_odd` at the odd diagonal constant, so $c=\Theta_n$ at every $n$ ([r1745](../reviews/REVIEW_r1745.md), and [r1746](../reviews/REVIEW_r1746.md), which refuted the first versions' $\theta_0(n)$ at odd $n$). **L227 is typed in full as `L227_full` in [L227Full.lean](../lean/HamiltonsCubic/Results/L227Full.lean)**: every Ricci-flat `blockOp` with symmetric blocks and two outer indices has $P(W)\le\tfrac{2\sqrt2}3\lVert W\rVert^3$, through `blockOp_normal_form`, `exists_diag_frame` and `L228_item3_of` at the odd constant $\Theta_5$. Its attainment is `L227_attained` in [L227Attain.lean](../lean/HamiltonsCubic/Results/L227Attain.lean): a unit Ricci-flat block operator with diagonal, hence commuting, blocks and $P=\tfrac{2\sqrt2}3$, built from $S^2\times S^3$ on `Fin 3 ⊕ Fin 2`, $\mathcal W_5$-membership being Ricci-flatness there and Bianchi L226's ([r1749](../reviews/REVIEW_r1749.md)). L227 is typed at the operator in full.

**What it gives.** **The $\lvert J\rvert=3$ step of [R27](../routes.md) at $n=5$, a model case of the route's mechanism outside the conjecture's range $n\ge12$.** *At two outer indices the relative frame of the blocks enters $P$ through one pairing, $\mathop{\mathrm{tr}}(UV^2)$, against a frame-free norm, and one alignment settles it. At $\lvert A\rvert\ge3$ outer indices the traces $\mathop{\mathrm{tr}}M_a$ are no longer forced equal, the fluctuations $Y_a=X_a-\tfrac1{\lvert A\rvert}\sum_bX_b$ carry a cubic term $\sum_a\mathop{\mathrm{tr}}Y_a^3$ under $\sum_aY_a=0$, which vanishes at $\lvert A\rvert=2$, and the Gram terms $\mathop{\mathrm{tr}}(Y_aY_b)$ enter with pairwise weights that the outer entries $K_{ab}$ leave unequal. The derivation found no single alignment that controls them ([derivation](../reviews/2026-09-15-I106-derivation.md), batch 6), a heuristic reading and not a derived obstruction.* At $n=5$ it locates no global maximiser, since padded $W_{\mathbb{CP}^2}$ has $\sqrt{3/2}>\Theta_5$.

#### Lemma L228, the $\lvert J\rvert=3$ step of R27 on two strata at every $n$: one block against a common frame, or one pair's difference against it

*Gate 2 passed: [r952](../reviews/REVIEW_r952.md) and [r953](../reviews/REVIEW_r953.md) on identical briefs, every item CONFIRMED by both, with Gate 1 (a), item 4's closing, the membership citation and the Lean scope repaired on their reports. Derived 2026-09-15 for [I106](../plan.md) ([derivation](../reviews/2026-09-15-I106-m3-derivation.md), batches 1 to 3), on Opus at the user's instruction in place of the Fable switch the loop requests for a route-deciding derivation. Contains [L227](second-order.md) as its case of two outer indices. Item 2's split of the cubic part, the shift to $Y_a$ and the alignment to a frame are kernel-checked in [L228Statement.lean](../lean/HamiltonsCubic/Results/L228Statement.lean). L228 inherits the two identities L57's block names as carrying L57: the vertex-matrix form of the cubic, which [L226](second-order.md)'s Gate 1 (b) reads at a coordinate-diagonal $W$, and the norm formula for $Z(v)$, which [L223](problem.md)'s block reports deriving.*

**Setting.** $n\ge4$, $\lvert J\rvert=3$, outer set $A$ with $m=\lvert A\rvert=n-3$, and $W\in\mathcal S_J\cap\mathcal W_n$ with blocks $M_a=t_aI+X_a$, $\mathop{\mathrm{tr}}X_a=0$, outer diagonal $K$ and $S=N-\tfrac12(\mathop{\mathrm{tr}}N)I$ as in [L226](second-order.md). Put $X=\sum_aX_a$, $\mu=\sum_at_a$ and $\beta_{ab}=\tfrac32\big(K_{ab}-t_a-t_b-\tfrac12\mu\big)$.

**Statement.**
1. *$P(W)=2\mathop{\mathrm{tr}}X^3+3\sum_a\mathop{\mathrm{tr}}(XX_a^2)+\sum_a\mathop{\mathrm{tr}}X_a^3+\sum_{a\ne b}\beta_{ab}\mathop{\mathrm{tr}}(X_aX_b)+P_0(t,K)$ and $\lVert W\rVert^2=\lvert X\rvert^2+\sum_a\lvert X_a\rvert^2+N_0(t,K)$, with $P_0,N_0$ depending on the scalars only.*
2. *For each $a$, with $R_a=\sum_{c\ne a}X_c$, $T_a=\sum_{c\ne a}X_c^2$ and $Y_a=X_a+\tfrac12R_a$: $P(W)=6\mathop{\mathrm{tr}}Y_a^3+\mathop{\mathrm{tr}}(Y_a\ell'_a)+c_a$ and $\lVert W\rVert^2=2\lvert Y_a\rvert^2+n_a$, where $\ell'_a=\tfrac32R_a^2+3T_a+2\sum_{c\ne a}\beta_{ac}X_c$ and $c_a,n_a$ do not depend on $X_a$.*
3. *If the blocks $X_c$, $c\ne a$, are simultaneously diagonal in one orthonormal frame of $\mathbb R^J$, then $P(W)\le\Theta_n\lVert W\rVert^3$.*
4. *For distinct $a,b\in A$, so $m\ge2$, if the blocks $X_c$, $c\notin\{a,b\}$, and $X_a+X_b$ are simultaneously diagonal in one orthonormal frame, then $P(W)\le\Theta_n\lVert W\rVert^3$.*

*In items 3 and 4 the value $\Theta_n$ is attained in $\mathcal D_n\subseteq\mathcal S_J$. At $m\le2$ item 3 covers every $W$.*

**Proof.**
1. **Item 1.** With $c=\tfrac12\mu$, $S=X-cI$ since $\mathop{\mathrm{tr}}N=3\mu$. For traceless $3\times3$ $Z$, Cayley–Hamilton gives $\det(Z-cI)=\tfrac13\mathop{\mathrm{tr}}Z^3+\tfrac12c\lvert Z\rvert^2-c^3$ and $\mathop{\mathrm{adj}}(tI+Z)=Z^2-tZ+\big(t^2-\tfrac12\lvert Z\rvert^2\big)I$, so in L226's item 3: $\mathop{\mathrm{tr}}S^3+3\det S=2\mathop{\mathrm{tr}}X^3-\tfrac34\mu\lvert X\rvert^2-\tfrac34\mu^3$, $\mathop{\mathrm{tr}}M_a^3=3t_a^3+3t_a\lvert X_a\rvert^2+\mathop{\mathrm{tr}}X_a^3$, $3\mathop{\mathrm{tr}}(S\mathop{\mathrm{adj}}M_a)=3\mathop{\mathrm{tr}}(XX_a^2)-3t_a\mathop{\mathrm{tr}}(XX_a)+\tfrac34\mu\lvert X_a\rvert^2-\tfrac92\mu t_a^2$, and $3K_{ab}\mathop{\mathrm{tr}}(M_aM_b)=9K_{ab}t_at_b+3K_{ab}\mathop{\mathrm{tr}}(X_aX_b)$. With $\lvert X\rvert^2=\sum_{a,b}\mathop{\mathrm{tr}}(X_aX_b)$ and $\mathop{\mathrm{tr}}(XX_a)=\sum_b\mathop{\mathrm{tr}}(X_aX_b)$, the coefficient of $\lvert X_a\rvert^2$ is $-\tfrac34\mu+3t_a-3t_a+\tfrac34\mu=0$ and that of $\mathop{\mathrm{tr}}(X_aX_b)$ over ordered pairs $a\ne b$ is $-\tfrac34\mu-\tfrac32(t_a+t_b)+\tfrac32K_{ab}=\beta_{ab}$. The norm is L226's $\lvert S\rvert^2+\sum\lvert M_a\rvert^2+\sum_{a<b}K_{ab}^2$ with $\lvert S\rvert^2=\lvert X\rvert^2+\tfrac34\mu^2$ and $\lvert M_a\rvert^2=3t_a^2+\lvert X_a\rvert^2$.
2. **Item 2.** Fix everything but $X_a$. Expanding item 1 in $X_a$, $2\mathop{\mathrm{tr}}(X_a+R_a)^3$ contributes $2\mathop{\mathrm{tr}}X_a^3+6\mathop{\mathrm{tr}}(X_a^2R_a)+6\mathop{\mathrm{tr}}(X_aR_a^2)$, $3\sum_c\mathop{\mathrm{tr}}(XX_c^2)$ contributes $3\mathop{\mathrm{tr}}X_a^3+3\mathop{\mathrm{tr}}(X_a^2R_a)+3\mathop{\mathrm{tr}}(X_aT_a)$, $\sum_c\mathop{\mathrm{tr}}X_c^3$ contributes $\mathop{\mathrm{tr}}X_a^3$, and the Gram part $2\sum_{c\ne a}\beta_{ac}\mathop{\mathrm{tr}}(X_aX_c)$, each up to terms free of $X_a$. So $P=6\mathop{\mathrm{tr}}X_a^3+9\mathop{\mathrm{tr}}(X_a^2R_a)+\mathop{\mathrm{tr}}(X_a\ell_a)+\text{const}$ with $\ell_a=6R_a^2+3T_a+2\sum_{c\ne a}\beta_{ac}X_c$, and $\lVert W\rVert^2=2\lvert X_a\rvert^2+2\mathop{\mathrm{tr}}(X_aR_a)+\text{const}=2\lvert Y_a\rvert^2-\tfrac12\lvert R_a\rvert^2+\text{const}$. Substituting $X_a=Y_a-\tfrac12R_a$, cyclicity gives $6\mathop{\mathrm{tr}}(Y_a-\tfrac12R_a)^3+9\mathop{\mathrm{tr}}\big((Y_a-\tfrac12R_a)^2R_a\big)=6\mathop{\mathrm{tr}}Y_a^3-\tfrac92\mathop{\mathrm{tr}}(Y_aR_a^2)+\tfrac32\mathop{\mathrm{tr}}R_a^3$, **the two $\mathop{\mathrm{tr}}(Y_a^2R_a)$ terms cancelling**, and $\mathop{\mathrm{tr}}(X_a\ell_a)=\mathop{\mathrm{tr}}(Y_a\ell_a)-\tfrac12\mathop{\mathrm{tr}}(R_a\ell_a)$. Collecting, $\ell'_a=\ell_a-\tfrac92R_a^2$.
3. **Item 3.** Let $F$ be the common frame. $R_a$, $T_a$ and each $X_c$ with $c\ne a$ are diagonal in $F$, so $\ell'_a=F\mathop{\mathrm{diag}}(l)F^{\mathsf T}$. Write $Y_a=Q\mathop{\mathrm{diag}}(y)Q^{\mathsf T}$. Then $\mathop{\mathrm{tr}}(Y_a\ell'_a)=\sum_{i,k}(Q^{\mathsf T}F)_{ik}^2\,y_il_k$, the matrix of squared entries of the orthogonal $Q^{\mathsf T}F$ is doubly stochastic, and by Birkhoff's theorem the linear function is at most its value at a permutation matrix: $\mathop{\mathrm{tr}}(Y_a\ell'_a)\le\sum_iy_il_{\sigma(i)}$ for some permutation $\sigma$. Put $Y'=F\mathop{\mathrm{diag}}(y\circ\sigma^{-1})F^{\mathsf T}$. It has $Y_a$'s spectrum, so $\mathop{\mathrm{tr}}Y'=0$, $\lvert Y'\rvert=\lvert Y_a\rvert$ and $\mathop{\mathrm{tr}}Y'^3=\mathop{\mathrm{tr}}Y_a^3$, and $\mathop{\mathrm{tr}}(Y'\ell'_a)=\sum_ky_{\sigma^{-1}(k)}l_k\ge\mathop{\mathrm{tr}}(Y_a\ell'_a)$. Replace $X_a$ by $X_a'=Y'-\tfrac12R_a$, traceless and diagonal in $F$, keeping every other block and the scalars. The result $W'$ lies in $\mathcal S_J$, since every block entry carries a character trivial on $H_J$ ([block note](../reviews/2026-09-14-I104-block-J.md)), and in $\mathcal W_n$ by L226's items 2 and 3, the traceless blocks being free once the scalars satisfy item 2, and by item 2 it has $\lVert W'\rVert=\lVert W\rVert$ and $P(W')\ge P(W)$. Every block of $W'$ and $S'=X'-\tfrac12\mu I$ are diagonal in $F$, the rotation of $\mathbb R^J$ by $F^{\mathsf T}$ conjugates them all (any orthogonal matrix does, $\Lambda^2F$ acting on the frame $\varphi_{23},\varphi_{31},\varphi_{12}$ by $(\det F)F$), and it carries $W'$ to a coordinate-diagonal Weyl tensor of the same norm and value, since $P$, the norm and $\mathcal W_n$ are $O(n)$-invariant. [L57](problem.md) gives $P(W)\le P(W')\le\Theta_n\lVert W\rVert^3$.
4. **Item 4.** Fix everything but $X_a,X_b$ and put $U=X_a+X_b$, $V=X_a-X_b$ with $U$ and $\lvert V\rvert$ fixed. Then $X$ is fixed and $\lvert X_a\rvert^2+\lvert X_b\rvert^2=\tfrac12(\lvert U\rvert^2+\lvert V\rvert^2)$, so the norm is fixed. In item 1, $3\mathop{\mathrm{tr}}\big(X(X_a^2+X_b^2)\big)=\tfrac32\mathop{\mathrm{tr}}\big(X(U^2+V^2)\big)$, $\mathop{\mathrm{tr}}X_a^3+\mathop{\mathrm{tr}}X_b^3=\tfrac14\mathop{\mathrm{tr}}U^3+\tfrac34\mathop{\mathrm{tr}}(UV^2)$ by cyclicity, $\mathop{\mathrm{tr}}(X_aX_b)=\tfrac14(\lvert U\rvert^2-\lvert V\rvert^2)$, and the Gram terms with $c\notin\{a,b\}$ give $\sum_{c\ne a,b}\big((\beta_{ac}+\beta_{bc})\mathop{\mathrm{tr}}(UX_c)+(\beta_{ac}-\beta_{bc})\mathop{\mathrm{tr}}(VX_c)\big)$. So the value depends on the frame of $V$ only through $\mathop{\mathrm{tr}}(V^2Z)+\mathop{\mathrm{tr}}(VL)$ with $Z=\tfrac32X+\tfrac34U$ and $L=\sum_{c\ne a,b}(\beta_{ac}-\beta_{bc})X_c$, both diagonal in the common frame $F$. With $V=Q\mathop{\mathrm{diag}}(\nu)Q^{\mathsf T}$ this is $\sum_{i,k}(F^{\mathsf T}Q)_{ik}^2\big(z_i\nu_k^2+l_i\nu_k\big)$, linear in one doubly stochastic matrix, so by Birkhoff's theorem some permutation $\pi$ has it at most $\sum_i\big(z_i\nu_{\pi(i)}^2+l_i\nu_{\pi(i)}\big)$, and $V'=F\mathop{\mathrm{diag}}(\nu\circ\pi)F^{\mathsf T}$, which has $V$'s spectrum, attains that value and makes $X_a'=\tfrac12(U+V')$ and $X_b'=\tfrac12(U-V')$ traceless and diagonal in $F$ at the same norm and no smaller value. $W'$ lies in $\mathcal S_J\cap\mathcal W_n$ as in item 3, and item 3's last two sentences finish. $\square$

**Attainment.** $\mathcal D_n=\mathcal S_{\{i\}}\subseteq\mathcal S_J$, since $H_J$ is a subgroup of the sign changes ([block note](../reviews/2026-09-14-I104-block-J.md)), and L57's maximiser has value $\Theta_n$.

**Gate 1.** (a) *Trusted values.* Item 1's cubic agrees with the trusted $Q$ of [curvature_operator.py](../scripts/lib/curvature_operator.py) at $n=5,6,7$ to ten digits, and item 1's norm with L226's block norm to ten digits, compared formula to formula ([screen_I106.py](../scripts/misc/screen_I106.py)), and item 2's prediction for a random rotation of $Y_a$ agrees with it at $n=6,7$ to ten digits, the norm unchanged to $10^{-14}$ ([screen_I106_m3.py](../scripts/misc/screen_I106_m3.py)). (b) *A second route.* At $m=2$ item 3 is [L227](second-order.md), proved there by the change of variables $U=X_1\pm X_2$ and an alignment of $V$ against $U$. (c) *A screen.* The move of item 3 raised $P$ at fixed norm at $n=6,7$ and left every block commuting to $3\cdot10^{-16}$. A screen of a derived move. (d) *At $m=1$* item 3 covers every $W$ and item 1 reads $P=6\mathop{\mathrm{tr}}X_1^3$ at $\lVert W\rVert^2=2\lvert X_1\rvert^2$, since $t_1=\mu=0$ and the absence of $K$ make $P_0=N_0=0$, the $n=4$ case of [L225](second-order.md).

**Lean.** `cubic_split` is item 2's expansion of the cubic part at one block, over `Fin (m + 1)`, `complete_square` and `complete_square_norm` are the shift, and `align_to_frame` is the Birkhoff move of item 3 for any orthogonal $F$, built on [L227Alignment.lean](../lean/HamiltonsCubic/Results/L227Alignment.lean). All proved. Items 1 and 2 are proved at the operator: `L228_item1` and `L228_item1_ricci` (at any Ricci-flat $S$ on the $J$ rows, with $P_0$, $N_0$, $\beta$ explicit) in [L228Item1.lean](../lean/HamiltonsCubic/Results/L228Item1.lean), and `L228_item2` (at the block $a=0$ of `Fin (m + 1)`, $c_a$ and $n_a$ explicit in the other blocks) in [L228Item2.lean](../lean/HamiltonsCubic/Results/L228Item2.lean), [r1743](../reviews/REVIEW_r1743.md). The rotation to a coordinate-diagonal tensor and L57's use are proved at the operator for any diagonal constant $c$: `L57_block_of` in [L57Block.lean](../lean/HamiltonsCubic/Results/L57Block.lean) (a Ricci-flat `blockOp S M K` with $S$ and every $M_a$ diagonal has $P(W)\le c\lVert W\rVert^3$, $n=m+3\ge4$, whenever $c$ bounds the coordinate-diagonal case) and `L57_block_conj_of` in [L57BlockConj.lean](../lean/HamiltonsCubic/Results/L57BlockConj.lean) (the same when one orthogonal $F$ diagonalises $S$ and every $M_a$ at once), with instances `L57_block`, `L57_block_conj` at $c=\theta_0(n)$ for even $n$ and `L57_block_odd` at the odd diagonal constant, so $c=\Theta_n$ at every $n$ ([r1745](../reviews/REVIEW_r1745.md), and [r1746](../reviews/REVIEW_r1746.md), which refuted the first versions' $\theta_0(n)$ at odd $n$). Item 3 is `L228_item3_of` with instances `L228_item3` (even $n$) and `L228_item3_odd`, and `L57_block_le_two_of` is the remark that at $m\le2$ item 3 covers every $W$. Item 4 is `L228_item4_of` with even and odd instances in [L228Item4.lean](../lean/HamiltonsCubic/Results/L228Item4.lean) ([r1748](../reviews/REVIEW_r1748.md)), and the attainment of $\Theta_n$ in $\mathcal D_n$ is `block_sphere`, `L228_attained_even` ($\theta_0(n)$, even $n\ge4$) and `L228_attained_odd` (the odd constant, odd $n\ge5$) in [L228Attain.lean](../lean/HamiltonsCubic/Results/L228Attain.lean). The relabelling to arbitrary blocks is typed in [L228Relabel.lean](../lean/HamiltonsCubic/Results/L228Relabel.lean) (`blockP_perm`, `normSq_perm`, `ricci_perm`, and `L228_item2_any`, `L228_item3_any`, `L228_item4_any` at every block and pair, [r1751](../reviews/REVIEW_r1751.md)), so L228 is typed at the operator in full.

**What it gives.** **The $\lvert J\rvert=3$ step of [R27](../routes.md) on the stratum where all outer blocks but one share an eigenframe, and on the stratum where all but two do and the two blocks' sum joins that frame, at every $n$.** *The mechanism is that one block, shifted by half the sum of the others, meets the rest only through a linear pairing against a frame-free norm. The node itself stays open at $m\ge3$: a general $W$ lies in neither stratum, and reaching one by moves that do not lower $P$ at fixed norm is the remaining question ([derivation](../reviews/2026-09-15-I106-m3-derivation.md), batch 4).* It contains L227, which stands with its own proof.

#### Lemma L229, $G(2)$ at fixed total norm on the common-eigenvector stratum: the phase maximum is one squared distance, and the value is affine where it is attained

*Gate 2 passed: [r955](../reviews/REVIEW_r955.md) and [r956](../reviews/REVIEW_r956.md) on identical briefs, the mathematics CONFIRMED by both with no number, sign or conclusion changed, and Gate 1 (a), Gate 1 (b)'s interior, the attainment clause, the normalisation's two symmetries and step 5's openness repaired on their reports. **Both readers also reported this result's row in [INDEX-results.md](INDEX-results.md) as ending in a dangling fragment, and that finding is not applied**: `short name` is a column of that table and every row carries one. Derived 2026-09-16 for [I106](../plan.md) ([derivation](../reviews/2026-09-15-I106-m3-derivation.md), batch 14), on Opus at the user's instruction. It supersedes that derivation's batch 12, whose closed form was three cases of calculus, and it removes the four-amplitude comparison batch 13 left owed. The identity, the cap, the diagonal deficits, the boundary attainment and the affineness are kernel-checked in [L229Statement.lean](../lean/HamiltonsCubic/Results/L229Statement.lean), with the closing step standing as a named `sorry`.*

**Setting.** $G(2)$ at fixed total norm is the problem $\sup\Phi$ over traceless symmetric $3\times3$ matrices $\hat U,V$ with $\tfrac32\lvert\hat U\rvert^2+\tfrac12\lvert V\rvert^2=T$, where $$\Phi(\hat U,V)=\alpha\mathop{\mathrm{tr}}\hat U^3+\beta\mathop{\mathrm{tr}}(\hat UV^2)+\mathop{\mathrm{tr}}(\hat UA)+\mathop{\mathrm{tr}}(VC),\qquad\alpha=\tfrac{15}4,\quad\beta=\tfrac94,$$ and $A,C$ are diagonal in an orthonormal frame $F$ of $\mathbb R^J$. It is the step of [R27](../routes.md) that frees two outer blocks while the rest stay diagonal in $F$, with $\hat U$ and $V$ the shifted sum and the difference of the two blocks and only the total norm held ([derivation](../reviews/2026-09-15-I106-m3-derivation.md), batch 10). **The common-eigenvector stratum** is the set where $\hat U$ and $V$ share an eigenvector that is a vector of $F$, say $e_2$. There, with $R(\vartheta)=\begin{pmatrix}\cos\vartheta&\sin\vartheta\\\sin\vartheta&-\cos\vartheta\end{pmatrix}$ on $e_2^\perp$, $\hat U=-\tfrac{u_2}2I+pR(2\varphi)$ and $V=-\tfrac{v_2}2I+rR(2\chi)$ with $p,r\ge0$, and $d_A=\tfrac12(A_1-A_3)$, $d_C=\tfrac12(C_1-C_3)$, $\tilde a=A_2-\tfrac12(A_1+A_3)$, $\tilde c=C_2-\tfrac12(C_1+C_3)$. Batch 11's frustration condition is $d_Ad_Cv_2>0$, a condition on the product, and **two maps carry any triple satisfying it to the all-positive one at equal value and equal norm**: relabelling $e_1\leftrightarrow e_3$, which flips $d_A$ and $d_C$ together and fixes $u_2,v_2$, and $V\mapsto-V$ together with $C\mapsto-C$, which flips $v_2$ and $d_C$ together and leaves $\mathop{\mathrm{tr}}(\hat UV^2)$ and $\mathop{\mathrm{tr}}(VC)$ alone. **So take $d_A,d_C,v_2>0$.** Where $d_Ad_Cv_2\le0$, which covers $v_2=0$, $d_A=0$ and $d_C=0$, the phase problem is unfrustrated and batch 11 already puts the maximum at a diagonal pair ([derivation](../reviews/2026-09-15-I106-m3-derivation.md), batch 11). Write $$\Lambda=\frac{d_Ad_C}{\beta v_2},\qquad\rho_1=\frac{\beta v_2\,r}{d_A},\qquad\rho_2=\frac{\beta v_2\,p}{d_C},\qquad\mathcal A=\{z\in\mathbb C:\lvert\rho_1-\rho_2\rvert\le\lvert z\rvert\le\rho_1+\rho_2\}.$$

**Statement.** *On the common-eigenvector stratum, $\sup\Phi$ at fixed $T$ equals the supremum over the points of that stratum at which $\hat U$ and $V$ are both diagonal in $F$, and it is attained there.* The four steps:
1. *The phase sum of batch 11 is $g=2\Lambda\rho_2\cos\vartheta_1+2\Lambda\rho_1\cos\vartheta_2-2\Lambda\rho_1\rho_2\cos(\vartheta_1-\vartheta_2)$ with $\vartheta_1=2\varphi$, $\vartheta_2=2\chi$, and at every pair of phases $$\Lambda\big(1+\rho_1^2+\rho_2^2\big)-g=\Lambda\big\lvert1-\rho_2e^{i\vartheta_1}-\rho_1e^{i\vartheta_2}\big\rvert^2\ \ge\ 0 .$$*
2. *$\max_\vartheta g=\Lambda\big[1+\rho_1^2+\rho_2^2-\mathop{\mathrm{dist}}(1,\mathcal A)^2\big]$, with $\mathop{\mathrm{dist}}(1,\mathcal A)=\max\big(0,\,1-\rho_1-\rho_2,\,\lvert\rho_1-\rho_2\rvert-1\big)$.*
3. *A pair of blocks diagonal in $F$ is $\vartheta_1,\vartheta_2\in\{0,\pi\}$, carrying signs $\epsilon_1,\epsilon_2\in\{\pm1\}$, where $g=2\Lambda(\epsilon_1\rho_2+\epsilon_2\rho_1-\epsilon_1\epsilon_2\rho_1\rho_2)$ and the deficit is the exact square $\Lambda(1-\epsilon_1\rho_2-\epsilon_2\rho_1)^2$. So the best diagonal value is $\Lambda\big[1+\rho_1^2+\rho_2^2-\mathop{\mathrm{dist}}(1,\{\pm\rho_1\pm\rho_2\})^2\big]$, and it equals $\max_\vartheta g$ exactly when $1\notin\mathop{\mathrm{int}}\mathcal A$.*
4. *At fixed $u_2$ and $v_2>0$, in the variables $x=p^2$ and $y=r^2$, the value with the cap in place is affine, $$\Phi=\Big(\tfrac34\alpha u_2^3+\tilde au_2+\tilde cv_2+\tfrac34\beta u_2v_2^2+\frac{d_Ad_C}{\beta v_2}\Big)+\Big(\frac{\beta v_2d_A}{d_C}-3\alpha u_2\Big)x+\Big(\frac{\beta v_2d_C}{d_A}-\beta u_2\Big)y,$$ while the norm constraint is the segment $3x+y=B$, $x,y\ge0$, with $B=T-\tfrac94u_2^2-\tfrac34v_2^2$.*

**Proof.**
1. **Item 1.** Expanding $\lvert1-\rho_2e^{i\vartheta_1}-\rho_1e^{i\vartheta_2}\rvert^2$ gives $1+\rho_2^2+\rho_1^2-2\rho_2\cos\vartheta_1-2\rho_1\cos\vartheta_2+2\rho_1\rho_2\cos(\vartheta_1-\vartheta_2)$, the cross term because $\cos(\vartheta_1-\vartheta_2)=\cos\vartheta_1\cos\vartheta_2+\sin\vartheta_1\sin\vartheta_2$. Multiplying by $\Lambda$ and comparing with $g$ gives the identity, and $\Lambda>0$ gives the inequality.
2. **Item 2.** The set $\{\rho_2e^{i\vartheta_1}+\rho_1e^{i\vartheta_2}:\vartheta_1,\vartheta_2\}$ is the sum of two circles, which is the closed annulus $\mathcal A$, and by item 1 maximising $g$ is minimising the squared distance from $1$ to a point of $\mathcal A$. The distance from a point of modulus $1$ to $\mathcal A$ is $0$ when $\lvert\rho_1-\rho_2\rvert\le1\le\rho_1+\rho_2$, and otherwise the gap to the nearer boundary circle.
3. **Item 3.** At $\vartheta_j\in\{0,\pi\}$, $\cos\vartheta_j=\epsilon_j$ and $\cos(\vartheta_1-\vartheta_2)=\epsilon_1\epsilon_2$, which is the stated value of $g$, and item 1 with $\sin\vartheta_j=0$ gives the deficit $\Lambda(1-\epsilon_1\rho_2-\epsilon_2\rho_1)^2$. The four reals $\epsilon_1\rho_2+\epsilon_2\rho_1$ are $\pm(\rho_1+\rho_2)$ and $\pm(\rho_1-\rho_2)$, so they include both radii of $\mathcal A$. Since $\mathcal A$ is invariant under rotation about $0$ and $1$ is real and positive, the point of $\mathcal A$ nearest $1$ is the real number $\min\big(\rho_1+\rho_2,\max(1,\lvert\rho_1-\rho_2\rvert)\big)$, which is one of those four exactly when $1\notin\mathop{\mathrm{int}}\mathcal A$. Inside the interior the distance to $\mathcal A$ is $0$ while the distance to the four points is positive.
4. **Item 4.** $\Lambda\rho_2^2=\frac{\beta v_2d_A}{d_C}x$ and $\Lambda\rho_1^2=\frac{\beta v_2d_C}{d_A}y$, so the cap $\Lambda(1+\rho_1^2+\rho_2^2)$ is affine in $(x,y)$, and batch 11's phase-free part contributes $-3\alpha u_2x-\beta u_2y$ and terms in $u_2,v_2$ alone. The norm is $\tfrac94u_2^2+3x+\tfrac34v_2^2+y=T$.
5. **The conclusion.** Fix $u_2$ and $v_2>0$, write $I=\{3x+y=B,\ x,y\ge0\}$ for the norm segment, and let $E\subseteq I$ be where $1\in\mathcal A$ and $E^\circ\subseteq I$ where $1\in\mathop{\mathrm{int}}\mathcal A$. **At every point of $I$ outside $E^\circ$ the value is already a diagonal value**, by item 3. **And every point of $E^\circ$ is dominated by one such point**: $E$ is closed in $I$ because $\rho_1,\rho_2$ are continuous in $(x,y)$ and the two defining inequalities are weak, each of its connected components is a closed subinterval, the value is affine on $E$ by item 4 and so attains its maximum over that component at one of its two endpoints, and such an endpoint is either an endpoint of $I$, where $\rho_1\rho_2=0$ and $\mathcal A$ is a circle with empty interior, or a boundary point of $E$ in $I$, where $1\in\partial\mathcal A$. Either way it lies outside $E^\circ$. Every configuration of the stratum is therefore matched, at the same $T$, $u_2$ and $v_2$, by one with $\hat U$ and $V$ diagonal in $F$, so the two suprema are equal. **The openness step**: $E^\circ$ is cut out of $I$ by the two strict inequalities $\lvert\rho_1-\rho_2\rvert<1<\rho_1+\rho_2$ in functions continuous on $I$, so it is open in $I$, and an endpoint of a component of $E$ lying in the interior of $I$ is therefore a boundary point of $E$, where one of those inequalities is an equality and $1\in\partial\mathcal A$. **And the supremum is attained**: the diagonal points of the stratum at fixed $T$ form a closed subset of the norm sphere, which is compact, and $\Phi$ is continuous. $\square$

**Gate 1.** (a) *Trusted values.* **No check behind this lemma reaches the trusted curvature operator, and this clause claimed otherwise until [r955](../reviews/REVIEW_r955.md) and [r956](../reviews/REVIEW_r956.md) read the scripts.** Batch 11's reduction of the two-block value and both norms to $(u_2,p,\varphi,v_2,r,\chi)$ is checked against $\Phi$ evaluated on explicitly built $3\times3$ blocks, under numpy alone, to $10^{-14}$ ([screen_I106_G2_commonaxis.py](../scripts/misc/screen_I106_G2_commonaxis.py)). Items 1 to 3 are checked on $\Lambda,\rho_1,\rho_2$ drawn at random ([screen_I106_annulus.py](../scripts/misc/screen_I106_annulus.py)), the identity to $6\cdot10^{-14}$ over $200000$ draws, item 2 against a $1441^2$ phase grid to $9\cdot10^{-5}$, which is that grid's resolution, and item 3 to $1\cdot10^{-14}$, **so they assume the conversion of batch 11's $K_1,K_2,K_{12}$ into $\Lambda,\rho_1,\rho_2$ rather than checking it, and that conversion is exact by hand and screened nowhere**. Item 4's affineness alone is checked against batch 11's reduced formula, to $3\cdot10^{-14}$. (b) *A second route.* Item 2 reproduces batch 12's closed form $\tfrac12(ab/c+bc/a+ca/b)$ under the triangle condition and $a+b+c-2\min(a,b,c)$ outside it, derived there by eliminating one angle and maximising a concave function of the other, and the triangle condition, whose inequalities are strict, is $1\in\mathop{\mathrm{int}}\mathcal A$. (c) *A screen.* Over $400$ field cases the maximum of the free value along the norm segment equalled the maximum of the diagonal value on the same segment to $0$, the maximiser landing where the two formulas agree. A screen of a derived conclusion. (d) *Ceilings.* The cap dominates all four diagonal values by the four exact squares $\Lambda(1\mp(\rho_1+\rho_2))^2$ and $\Lambda(1\pm(\rho_1-\rho_2))^2$, so no diagonal value can breach it, and the identity of item 1 makes the cap unbreachable at any phases.

**Lean.** `phase_deficit` is item 1's identity and `phase_le_cap` the cap, `collinear_eq_phaseSum` and `collinear_deficit` are item 3's diagonal values and their squares, `cap_attained_of_boundary` is item 3's agreement on $\partial\mathcal A$, and `capValue_affine` is item 4. All proved on standard axioms. `diagonal_matches` types the conclusion of step 5 on the segment, as a named `sorry`. **Not typed**: the reduction of the block problem to this phase sum, which is [L226](second-order.md) and batch 11, and the passage from `diagonal_matches` back to blocks diagonal in $F$.

**What it gives.** **$G(2)$ at fixed total norm on the stratum where the two free blocks share an eigenvector of the field frame**, which is the stratum carrying [post F68](fence-posts.md)'s witness, and therefore the case that refuted the fixed-separate-norms form of the same claim. *The mechanism is that the deficit of the phase sum against a cap affine in the squared amplitudes is one squared distance in the plane, and the region where that cap is attained has its boundary exactly where a diagonal pair attains it too.* **Open**: $G(2)$ on the stratum where the two blocks share no eigenvector, and the induction from $G(2)$ to $G(m)$ ([derivation](../reviews/2026-09-15-I106-m3-derivation.md), batch 14). **That first stratum is now one commutator**: commuting each Lagrange equation with the other variable gives the ring identities $\mu K-\beta\{\hat U,K\}=[\hat U,C]$ and $[V,A]=3\alpha\{\hat U,K\}-3\mu K$, whose elimination is $3(\alpha-\beta)\mu K=3\alpha[\hat U,C]+\beta[V,A]$, and in $\hat U$'s eigenframe the first reads $(\mu+\beta u_k)K_{ij}=[\hat U,C]_{ij}$. *Batch 18's bound makes every $\mu+\beta u_k$ nonnegative, and it can vanish only where $u_i=u_j$ forces $K_{ij}=0$ anyway, so at every global maximum $[\hat U,V]=0$ if and only if $[\hat U,C]=0$, the moving block commuting with the field the rest of the configuration generates* ([derivation](../reviews/2026-09-15-I106-m3-derivation.md), batch 30, typed in [I106Criticality.lean](../lean/HamiltonsCubic/Results/I106Criticality.lean)). **With one external block the criterion is a commutator of the original blocks**: there $C=(\beta_{ac}-\beta_{bc})X_c$ and $A=4X_c^2+(\beta_{ac}+\beta_{bc}-\tfrac23\beta_{ab})X_c$ are both polynomials in that block, the shift $\tfrac23R$ drops out of $[\hat U,C]$, and the equivalence reads $[\hat U,V]=0$ iff $(\beta_{ac}-\beta_{bc})[X_a+X_b,X_c]=0$ (batch 31). **Gate 2 PASSED**, [r959](../reviews/REVIEW_r959.md) and [r960](../reviews/REVIEW_r960.md), independently and by hand, with nothing refuted and five wording defects corrected in place. *Two things the round added: the equivalence uses maximality only through batch 18's bound, so it holds at every critical point meeting that bound; and the bound's constant $2\beta/\sqrt6$ is sharp, attained at $\hat U\propto V\propto\mathop{\mathrm{diag}}(2,-1,-1)$ with $\lvert\hat U\rvert^2=T/2$, where the three denominators $\mu+\beta u_k$ are nevertheless strictly positive.* **The statement to aim at is therefore $[\hat U,C]=0$ at every global maximum, which closes the $\lvert J\rvert=3$ node outright rather than one stratum of it.** *Two levers toward it are closed. A condition on the fields CAN be criticality restated, since the fields are defined by the criticality equations, and substituting their Lagrange forms is the test that tells which ([post F69](fence-posts.md), **Gate 2 PASSED** on [r961](../reviews/REVIEW_r961.md) and [r962](../reviews/REVIEW_r962.md)). *An earlier version of this sentence said no condition on the fields is ever an input, which both readers refuted with the same counterexample: $[A,C]=0$ is a condition on the fields whose substitution is batch 29's quadratic rather than an identity, and it is this derivation's own standing hypothesis. Post F69 itself is correctly hedged and it is this propagated sentence that overreached.* And the simultaneous-conjugation orbit, which supplies the joint tangent direction $X=[\Omega,\hat U]$, $Y=[\Omega,V]$ that the joint second-order condition had never been given, yields exactly that condition evaluated there and a third lower bound on $\mu$ that excludes no non-commuting critical point the existing bounds TOGETHER do not already exclude, being redundant against the pair and against neither one alone* ([derivation](../reviews/2026-09-15-I106-m3-derivation.md), batch 33, **Gate 2 PASSED** on [r961](../reviews/REVIEW_r961.md) and [r962](../reviews/REVIEW_r962.md), one table verdict refuted and corrected). **And the three non-commuting critical points once recorded as surviving all three conditions do NOT survive batch 21**, refuted 2026-09-17 by [r973](../reviews/REVIEW_r973.md) and validated independently here ([screen_I106a_signature.py](../scripts/misc/screen_I106a_signature.py)). *Batch 21 requires the second variation to be non-positive on the whole $9$-dimensional tangent space, and it is INDEFINITE at all three*, signature $6/3$ at $(1,-3,2)$ with $\mu=11.60060$, $6/3$ at $(-1,-3,4)$ with $\mu=24.43870$ and $7/2$ at $(19,-20,1)$ with $\mu=762.38453$, the top eigenvalues being $+10.27$, $+2.78$ and $+79.85$. **They are critical points that are not local maxima, so they were never obstacles to this node.** *What they cleared was the threshold $\mu\ge\alpha\lvert u_{\min}\rvert$, which is batch 21 read at ONE direction and is strictly weaker than batch 21 itself, so the column recording them overstated what it tested.* **What stands is the value comparison at equal total norm**, and it now has no surviving point to be tested against until one is produced.

#### Lemma L230, the singular locus of the entrywise square strictly contains the split locus, and does so from $k=4$

*Gate 2 OWED, dispatched nowhere yet, and cited nowhere as settled. Derived 2026-09-17 for [I106b](../plan.md) under [R28](../routes.md), on Opus. The mechanism is proved by hand and needs no computation; the rank is screened rather than derived ([screen_I106b_zero_diagonal.py](../scripts/misc/screen_I106b_zero_diagonal.py)).*

**Setting.** $\varphi_r:O(k)\to B_k$ is the entrywise square $Q\mapsto Q^{\circ2}$, whose image is the orthostochastic set and whose singular points are those where $d\varphi_r$ fails to be injective. An orthogonal matrix **splits** when, up to permutations of rows and columns, it is a direct sum of two smaller orthogonal matrices. [R28](../routes.md) excludes an interior tangency, concludes the maximum sits on the boundary of the image, and then identifies that boundary with the split locus, which is the previous rung of the induction.

**Statement, as corrected at Gate 2 2026-09-17** ([r974](../reviews/REVIEW_r974.md), [r975](../reviews/REVIEW_r975.md), both REFUTING the quantifier this block first carried). *Let $k\in\{3,4\}$ and let $Q\in O(k)$ have $Q_{ii}=0$ for every $i$. Then $Q$ is a singular point of $\varphi_r$. At $k=3$ no such $Q$ has all its off-diagonal entries nonzero. At $k=4$ one does, it is a singular point, and it does not split.* **Every clause of that is now a Lean proposition**, the singularity through an explicit nonzero skew $\Omega$ in the kernel of $d\varphi_r$ and the non-splitting through the source's own two-sided definition, so the statement is typed rather than approximated by its constants. *Two quantities are recorded but deliberately NOT part of the statement, being screened rather than proved: the rank is $5$ against $\dim O(4)=6$ with a one-dimensional kernel, and the $k=3$ clause's own singularity follows from the counting below rather than from a declaration.* **So "singular implies splits" is a theorem at $k=3$ and FALSE at $k=4$.** *The first version of this statement read "at every $k$" and that is FALSE, not merely unproved*: the counting below gives a drop only while $(k-1)^2-k<\tbinom k2$, which fails from $k=5$, and both reviewers exhibited regular points of exactly the declared shape, r974 an exact $k=5$ circulant and r975 the normalised order-$6$ Paley conference matrix.

**Proof.**
1. **The mechanism.** The tangent space to $O(k)$ at $Q$ is $\{Q\Omega:\Omega^{\mathsf T}=-\Omega\}$, and $\varphi_r$ has differential $Z\mapsto2\,Q\circ Z$ at $Q$, so a tangent vector maps to $2\,Q\circ(Q\Omega)$. Its $i$-th diagonal entry is $2Q_{ii}(Q\Omega)_{ii}$, which vanishes for every $\Omega$ once $Q_{ii}=0$. The image of $d\varphi_r$ therefore lies in the zero-diagonal subspace, on top of the zero row sums and zero column sums that every tangent to the bistochastic matrices already satisfies. That is $k$ further linear conditions on a space of dimension $(k-1)^2$, so the image has dimension at most $(k-1)^2-k$, and $d\varphi_r$ fails to be injective as soon as $(k-1)^2-k<\tbinom k2$. **That inequality is $\tfrac12(k^2-5k+2)<0$, so it holds at $k=3$ and $k=4$ and AT NO LARGER $k$**, the values running $1<3$, $5<6$, then $11>10$, $19>15$, $29>21$. *The bound itself is exact rather than lossy: the $k$ diagonal functionals are independent of the row-sum and column-sum functionals and of each other at every $k\ge3$, so the dimension is exactly $(k-1)^2-k$, verified as a rank count at $k=3$ to $8$. It is the MECHANISM that runs out, not the estimate.* *Neither orthogonality of $Q$ nor skewness of $\Omega$ is used in the vanishing itself.*
4. **And beyond $k=4$ the conclusion is false, by a lemma with its own witnesses** ([r975](../reviews/REVIEW_r975.md)). *Let $k\ge3$ and let $Q\in O(k)$ be SYMMETRIC with zero diagonal and every off-diagonal entry nonzero. Then $Q$ is REGULAR.* Since every off-diagonal $Q_{ij}$ is nonzero, a kernel element forces $Q\Omega=D$ diagonal, so $\Omega=QD$, and skewness reads $d_iQ_{ij}=-Q_{ij}d_j$, giving $d_i=-d_j$ at every $i\ne j$, which with three indices forces $d=0$. **The normalised order-$6$ Paley conference matrix satisfies the hypotheses**, and all $12$ normalised order-$6$ conference matrices are symmetric with $\mathop{\mathrm{rank}}d\varphi_r=15=\dim O(6)$, checked here. *No such symmetric matrix exists at $k=4$, which is why the lemma does not collide with Theorem 2.6.* **r974 adds an exact $k=5$ circulant**, first row proportional to $(0,1,-\tfrac{1+\sqrt3}2,1,1+\sqrt3)$, zero diagonal, all other entries nonzero, and injective.
2. **Emptiness at $k=3$.** Write the first two rows of a zero-diagonal $3\times3$ matrix as $(0,a,b)$ and $(c,0,d)$. Their inner product is $0\cdot c+a\cdot0+bd=bd$, so orthogonality forces $b=0$ or $d=0$ and some off-diagonal entry vanishes. **This is what makes [Theorem 2.4](../references/au-yeung-poon-1979-orthostochastic-numerical-ranges/notes.md) consistent with Theorem 2.6 rather than in tension with it**: the second type is empty at $k=3$.
3. **The witness at $k=4$.** Take $Q=3^{-1/2}M$ with $M$ the matrix of rows $(0,1,1,1)$, $(1,0,-1,1)$, $(1,1,0,-1)$ and $(1,-1,1,0)$. Then $MM^{\mathsf T}=3I$ by inspection of the ten pairings, so $Q\in O(4)$, its diagonal is zero, and every other entry is $\pm3^{-1/2}$. Its image is $Q^{\circ2}=\tfrac13(J-I)$, which is bistochastic and has no zero off the diagonal, so its support graph is connected and it does not split. **By item 1 it is singular, and by construction it is not in the split locus.** $\square$

**Gate 1.** (a) *Trusted values.* The generic control runs the same code path and returns $\mathop{\mathrm{rank}}d\varphi_r=6=\dim O(4)$ at all $200$ draws, so the witness's $5$ is a drop rather than a defect of the routine. (b) *A second route.* The rank is bounded above analytically by item 1 at $(k-1)^2-k=5$, and the screen returns exactly $5$, so the derived ceiling and the measurement agree rather than merely being consistent. (c) *A screen.* The witness's orthogonality is checked at $2.2\cdot10^{-16}$, its zero diagonal exactly, its minimum off-diagonal modulus at $0.577350$, and its image against $\tfrac13(J-I)$ at $1.1\cdot10^{-16}$, so the object is screened rather than assumed. (d) *Ceilings.* $5<6=\dim O(4)$ is the comparison that makes the point singular, and $6$ is the ceiling the generic control attains.

**Lean.** [I106bSingularLocus.lean](../lean/HamiltonsCubic/Results/I106bSingularLocus.lean). `dphi_diag_eq_zero` is item 1's vanishing, stated at every size and with `Q i i = 0` as its only hypothesis, so it takes neither orthogonality nor skewness. `witness_mul_transpose` is $MM^{\mathsf T}=3I$, `witness_diag` the zero diagonal and `witness_offdiag_ne` that every off-diagonal entry is nonzero. `no_second_type_at_three` is item 2. **THE SINGULARITY AT $k=4$ IS TYPED RATHER THAN SCREENED**: `Om` is the explicit kernel element $\Omega=\mathop{\mathrm{diag}}(-1,1,1,1)M$, with `Om_skew` and `Om_ne_zero` making it a genuine nonzero tangent direction, `witness_mul_Om` the diagonality of $M\Omega$, and `witness_singular` the vanishing $2(M\circ M\Omega)=0$. *So the differential is exhibited as non-injective instead of the rank being measured, and the kernel is found rather than assumed: every off-diagonal $M_{ij}$ being nonzero forces $M\Omega$ diagonal, and $M M^{\mathsf T}=3I$ with skewness then pins $\Omega$ up to scale.* `image_offdiag_ne` carries the non-splitting as the statement that every off-diagonal entry of $M\circ M$ is nonzero. Standard axioms, no `sorry`. **NOT typed**: the rank drop at GENERAL $k$, which item 1 bounds at $(k-1)^2-k$ without pinning, and the passage from a connected support graph to the absence of any permuted direct-sum decomposition.

**What it does to R28, restated after Gate 2.** *The rung's step reads exclude the interior tangency, therefore the maximum is on the boundary, therefore it is on the split locus, which is the previous rung.* **The last arrow fails, and this block's first argument for that was a NON SEQUITUR**, both reviewers catching it: R28 needs the frontier of the IMAGE and the counting above describes the singular locus of the MAP, and those are different sets here. *At $k=6$ the point $\tfrac15(J-I)$ is a boundary point of the image every one of whose preimages is REGULAR, so "boundary points are images of singular points" is false; and a literal reading of boundary inside $B_k$ is vacuous anyway, the image having empty interior since $\tbinom k2<(k-1)^2$ at every $k\ge3$.* **The conclusion survives by a replacement argument carrying no rank at all** ([r975](../reviews/REVIEW_r975.md)). $\mathop{\mathrm{tr}}\varphi_r(Q)=\sum_iQ_{ii}^2\ge0$ on the image, vanishing exactly at images of zero-diagonal $Q$, and it is nonconstant since $\varphi_r(I)=I$ has trace $k$. *So a point of the image with $\mathop{\mathrm{tr}}=0$ lies in the boundary RELATIVE TO THE AFFINE HULL, because an affine function attaining a local minimum at a relatively interior point would be constant.* **And that face holds non-split points**: a split image carries at least $2(k-1)$ zeros while a zero-diagonal image with all other entries nonzero carries exactly $k$, and $k<2(k-1)$ at every $k\ge3$. *Realised at $k=4$ and at $k=6$, and open at $k=5$.* **So the obstruction is a whole FACE of the image rather than a second singular stratum**, which matters because the singular-locus framing suggests enumerating strata and the face argument shows there is nothing to enumerate. *[I106b](../plan.md)'s debt is therefore the tangency exclusion plus this face, and the rung is non-uniform, one failing rung sufficing for that.*

#### From [I96](../plan.md): the constants of the isolation estimate, and why it needs the diagonal part to be near the balanced product

**Working notes, 2026-09-13, screened not derived.** *The off-diagonal part of criticality at a split $W=D+N$ is*
$$(2L_D-\theta)N=-\pi^\perp(N\circ N),$$
*which follows from [Lemma L214](#lemma-l214-the-frame-split-two-exact-relations-in-every-frame-and-one-inequality-under-the-second-order-cap)'s structural input alone: $\pi^\perp(D\circ D)=0$ because $D\circ D\in\mathcal D_n$, and $\pi^\perp(D\circ N)=D\circ N$ because $L_D$ preserves the isotypic summands.* **So a linear part bounded below by $\gamma$ transverse to its kernel, against a quadratic part bounded above by $\kappa$, gives $\lVert N\rVert\ge\gamma/\kappa$ or $N=0$**, which is quantitative isolation.

**The two constants, measured on an explicitly built $\mathcal D_n$** ([screen_isolation_constants.py](../scripts/runs/screen_isolation_constants.py), $\mathcal D_n$ as the symmetric hollow zero-row-sum sectional matrices, dimension $n(n-3)/2$ confirmed at $9$ and $20$).

| $n$ | $\theta$ | halves on $\mathcal D_n^\perp$ | orbit $p^2$ | $\min\lvert2\lambda-1\rvert$ off the kernel | $\gamma=\theta\cdot$that | $\kappa\ge$ | $\gamma/\kappa\le$ |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| $6$ | $1.054092553$ | $18$ | $9$ | $1/2$ | $0.527046$ | $0.732371$ | $0.719644$ |
| $8$ | $1.145643924$ | $16$ | $16$ | $1/3$ | $0.381881$ | $0.459387$ | $0.831284$ |

**The measured gap is $2/(n-2)$ at both dimensions**, twice the record's own $L_e$-eigenvalue gap $\tfrac1{n-2}$ at [L149](#lemma-l149-no-direction-is-both-low-margin-and-high-operator-norm-so-the-bound-is-uniform)'s block, *which corroborates the value and does not establish the closed form: two floating-point measurements are two points* ([r928](../reviews/REVIEW_r928.md)).

> **WHAT THIS TABLE CANNOT SUPPORT, AND THE BLOCK FIRST CLAIMED IT DID** ([r928](../reviews/REVIEW_r928.md)). *The estimate needs $\lVert\pi^\perp(N\circ N)\rVert\le\kappa\lVert N\rVert^2$, so it needs $\kappa$ from ABOVE, and the script measures it from below by sampling and ascent, which the table labels honestly as $\kappa\ge$ and $\gamma/\kappa\le$.* **So no radius follows from these numbers at all.** *Pushed further, at $4000$ samples and six ascents of $140$ steps over three seeds, $\kappa$ rises to $0.496876$ at $n=8$ and $0.901624$ at $n=6$ and is still climbing with the seed, dropping $\gamma/\kappa$ to $0.769$ and $0.585$: at $n=6$ that is already below the $0.707$ witness with no hypothesis on $D$ at all.* **What [I96](../plan.md) owes is therefore an UPPER bound on $\kappa$, and the perturbation step besides.**

> **AND THE ESTIMATE NEEDS A VANISHING EXCESS AS AN EXPLICIT HYPOTHESIS, WHICH THIS TABLE SHOWS FAILING AT $n=6$** ([r928](../reviews/REVIEW_r928.md)). *The kernel of $2L_D-\theta$ is the whole $\tfrac12$-eigenspace of $L_e$, which the table gives as $18$ dimensions at $n=6$ against a gauge orbit of $9$. So nine kernel directions there are not gauge, and for an $N$ with a component in them the estimate delivers nothing.* **At $n=8$ and $n=12$ the two coincide**, which is what the excess measurement says and why it is the route's gating hypothesis rather than a remark.

**That $\gamma$ is a function of $D$ is true by construction and is NOT what the numbers showed**: $\gamma$ is defined as the gap of $2L_D-\theta$ transverse to its kernel, so it varies with $D$ whatever any measurement says ([r928](../reviews/REVIEW_r928.md) refuted the block's earlier reasoning here while confirming its conclusion). *The claim that a padded $S^2\times S^2$ has a smaller gap of its own remains unmeasured.* **So the statement [I96](../plan.md) should aim at is local to a neighbourhood of the balanced product, with a vanishing excess and an upper bound on $\kappa$ among its hypotheses**, and the distance $\lVert N\rVert=\sqrt{1/2}$ to padded $\mathbb{CP}^2$ is the ceiling any correct radius must respect, that value being the record's through three independent routes.

#### Lemma L123, a zero-diagonal operator contributes nothing to a single-character Rayleigh quotient

**Let $F$ be an orthonormal frame of $\mathbb R^n$, $\Gamma=\{\mathop{\mathrm{diag}}(\pm1)\}\subset O(n)$ its full sign group, and $X\in\mathrm{Sym}^2(\Lambda^2)$ a vector lying in a single $\Gamma$-character space. If $R$ has zero diagonal in the frame $F$, then $\langle X,R\circ X\rangle=0$.**

**Proof.** $\varphi_\alpha\odot\varphi_\beta$ carries the character $\chi_S$ with $S=\alpha\triangle\beta$, and a symmetric difference of two $2$-sets is empty exactly when the sets coincide, so **the trivial character space is precisely the diagonal entries**, and $R$ with zero diagonal has no trivial component: $R=\sum_{\chi\ne1}R_\chi$. $\Gamma$ acts on each character space by a **scalar**, and $\circ$ is $\Gamma$-**equivariant**, so $g\cdot(R_\chi\circ X)=(g\cdot R_\chi)\circ(g\cdot X)=\chi(g)\chi_X(g)\,(R_\chi\circ X)$: the product lies in the character $\chi\chi_X$. *Equivariance is the load-bearing step and an earlier wording here credited bilinearity alone ([r355](../reviews/REVIEW_r355.md)).* The pairing $\mathop{\mathrm{tr}}(XY)$ is $\Gamma$-invariant, so distinct isotypics are orthogonal; and $\chi\chi_X=\chi_X$ forces $\chi=1$ by cancellation in the dual group. Every term is therefore orthogonal to $X$. $\square$

*Gate 2, [r353](../reviews/REVIEW_r353.md) and [r354](../reviews/REVIEW_r354.md) on identical briefs, each re-deriving it before reading the derivation. Machine-checked by averaging over $\Gamma$: `L123` in the coordinate frame and `L123_frame` in an arbitrary orthonormal one, in [L123Avg.lean](../lean/HamiltonsCubic/Results/L123Avg.lean).* **It is more general than the use it was found for**: it holds for any single character space, so it transports [Lemma L122](#lemma-l122-the-pair-block-is-a-graph-laplacian-of-the-sectional-matrix)'s pair block to an arbitrary frame as well, and it is unaffected if $L_W$ carries a Weyl projection. **The full sign group is required**, on $\Gamma\cap SO(n)$ complementary characters fuse at $n=4,6,8$ ([Lemma L120](#lemma-l120-l_m-is-block-diagonal-over-the-sign-characters-at-every-diagonal-critical-point)).

**What it does not give.** It kills the cross-term $\langle X,L_RX\rangle$ in the frame decomposition $L_W=L_{\mathop{\mathrm{diag}}(M)}+L_R$, and **nothing else**. In particular it does not license reading [Lemma L121](#lemma-l121-the-4-set-block-its-spectrum-and-one-necessary-condition-per-4-subset)'s inequality off a non-eigenframe: [Lemma L24](#lemma-l24-second-order-optimality-as-an-eigenvalue-bound-the-canonical-statement) also requires $X\perp W$, which fails there, and the resulting projection defect is a separate correction. *That conflation is recorded in [DERIVE-mx1.md](../reviews/DERIVE-mx1.md).*

**Scope corrections to [Lemma L121](#lemma-l121-the-4-set-block-its-spectrum-and-one-necessary-condition-per-4-subset) and [Lemma L122](#lemma-l122-the-pair-block-is-a-graph-laplacian-of-the-sectional-matrix), from the same audit.** L121 holds under **diagonality alone**, verified from the structure constants, with no row sum of $M$ entering, although this file states it for a diagonal *critical* point; the wider scope is what makes it applicable to $\mathop{\mathrm{diag}}(M)$ in a frame where $W$ is not diagonal. For L122 the bracketed form is likewise diagonality-only, **but the rewriting as $-\tfrac12\Delta^{(jk)}-\tfrac12M_{jk}\mathrm{Id}$ and the identification of the Ricci cut with $\mathbf 1^\perp$ both require Ricci-flatness**, so the scope line "not Ricci-flatness" over-reaches and is corrected here. *Ricci-flatness of $W$ reads $\sum_bM_{ab}=0$, a condition on frame-diagonal entries only, so $\mathop{\mathrm{diag}}(M)\in\mathcal W_n$ in every frame and the split sits inside the Weyl space.*

**And [Lemma L122](#lemma-l122-the-pair-block-is-a-graph-laplacian-of-the-sectional-matrix)'s bound is attained.** The orbit direction is a $\Delta^{(jk)}$-eigenvector at exactly $-(\theta+M_{jk})$, so the lemma's content is that the orbit direction minimises the Rayleigh quotient on $\mathbf 1^\perp$; it is strict only in the orbit-free blocks. The orbit needs no separate exclusion, [Lemma L24](#lemma-l24-second-order-optimality-as-an-eigenvalue-bound-the-canonical-statement)'s bound holding on all of $W^\perp$ with equality along it.

#### Lemma L124, on the half-flat four-set family, $\theta=9\det V_+$, and $\theta^2\le\tfrac32\rho^2$ with equality exactly at $\pm W_{\mathbb{CP}^2}$

*[r365](../reviews/REVIEW_r365.md) and [r366](../reviews/REVIEW_r366.md), identical briefs, agreeing on every verdict. The first version of this lemma claimed $\Sigma=\tfrac32\rho^2$ and three consequences; **the equality and all three are withdrawn**, and what stands is below.*

**Fix a four-subset $S=\{i,j,k,l\}$ and write $A=M_{ij}+M_{kl}$, $B=M_{ik}+M_{jl}$, $C=M_{il}+M_{jk}$ for the opposite-pair sums, $\Sigma:=A^2+B^2+C^2-AB-BC-CA$, and $V_+:=W|_{\Lambda^2_+\langle S\rangle}$ with $\rho^2:=\lVert W|_{\Lambda^2\langle S\rangle}\rVert^2$. Call $W$ *half-flat at $S$* when $W|_{\Lambda^2\langle S\rangle}$ annihilates $\Lambda^2_-\langle S\rangle$.**

> **(i) If $A+B+C=0$ then $\Sigma=\tfrac32(A^2+B^2+C^2)$**, an identity in three reals, carrying no information about $W$. *At a half-flat four-set the hypothesis holds, by first Bianchi together with half-flatness.*
>
> **(ii) Half-flat at $S$ $\Rightarrow$ $A,B,C$ are the DIAGONAL ENTRIES of $V_+$ in the basis $\omega_1=(e_{ij}+e_{kl})/\sqrt2$, $\omega_2=(e_{ik}-e_{jl})/\sqrt2$, $\omega_3=(e_{il}+e_{jk})/\sqrt2$**, whence
> $$\Sigma=\tfrac32\lVert\mathop{\mathrm{diag}}V_+\rVert^2\le\tfrac32\rho^2,\qquad\text{equality iff the three opposite-pair planes are $W$-invariant.}$$
>
> **(iii) THE RIGIDITY, and it is a statement about ONE POINT. On the half-flat family supported at one four-set, $\theta=9\det V_+$; the sharp bound $\lvert\det X\rvert\le\lVert X\rVert^3/(3\sqrt6)$ on traceless symmetric $3\times3$ (machine-checked as `det_sq_le` in [L124Det.lean](../lean/HamiltonsCubic/Results/L124Det.lean)) then gives $\theta^2\le\tfrac32\rho^2$, with equality exactly at $\pm W_{\mathbb{CP}^2}$, which is moreover the family's ONLY critical point, since $\nabla\det$ on the sphere forces two equal eigenvalues.**
>
> **THE FORM "$\theta^2\le\Sigma$" IS A NON SEQUITUR AND IS WITHDRAWN** ([r368](../reviews/REVIEW_r368.md)). *$\Sigma$ is frame-dependent and $\theta$ is not, the very test stated two boxes below, so the ordering holds only in $V_+$'s own eigenframe, where $\Sigma=\tfrac32\rho^2$. Rotate $V_+$ to zero diagonal, which the box below supplies, and $\Sigma=0<\theta^2=\tfrac32$.*

**Proof.** *(i)* is $AB+BC+CA=\tfrac12[(A+B+C)^2-\sum A^2]$. *(ii)* Half-flatness makes $\Lambda^2_-\langle S\rangle$ the kernel, so by symmetry the image lies in $\Lambda^2_+\langle S\rangle$; writing $e_{ij}=(\omega_1+\bar\omega_1)/\sqrt2$ and $e_{kl}=(\omega_1-\bar\omega_1)/\sqrt2$ gives $M_{ij}=M_{kl}=\tfrac12(V_+)_{11}$, so $A=(V_+)_{11}$, and likewise for $B,C$. Then $\rho^2=\lVert V_+\rVert^2=\sum A^2+2\sum_{a<b}(V_+)_{ab}^2$. *(iii)* On this family $P$ is an $SO(3)$-invariant cubic on traceless symmetric $3\times3$, hence a multiple of $\det$; the constant is pinned at $V_+=(2,-1,-1)/\sqrt6$, where $\det=1/(3\sqrt6)$ and $\theta=\sqrt{3/2}$, giving $9$. The determinant bound's equality case is $X\propto(2,-1,-1)$. $\square$

> **THE EQUALITY $\Sigma=\tfrac32\rho^2$ IS FALSE, AND ONE LINE KILLS IT.** $\Sigma$ is **frame-dependent** while $\rho^2$ and half-flatness are **frame-invariant**, so no identity between them can hold under invariant hypotheses. *Every traceless symmetric $3\times3$ is orthogonally similar to one with zero diagonal, and $A,B,C$ are that diagonal: padded $\mathbb{CP}^2$ rotated inside $\langle S\rangle$ stays critical at $\theta=\sqrt{3/2}$, stays half-flat, keeps $\rho^2=1$, and has $\Sigma=0.656$ against a predicted $\tfrac32$.*

> **AND THE HYPOTHESES ARE INCOMPATIBLE WHERE [Lemma L121](#lemma-l121-the-4-set-block-its-spectrum-and-one-necessary-condition-per-4-subset) APPLIES.** Pair-basis diagonality together with half-flatness forces every $M_{ab}=0$ on $S$, hence $W|_S=0$. *L121 is about $L_M$ on $\mathrm{Sym}^2(\Lambda^2)$ and supplies no invariant plane of $W$ on $\Lambda^2$; reading one off it is a category error.*

**Three withdrawn readings, each refuted twice over.** *(a)* That the padded $\mathbb{CP}^2$ equality is **a collision of independent facts**: part (iii) shows $\theta$ and $\Sigma$ are functions of the same spectrum, $\Sigma\equiv\tfrac32$ across the family while $\theta^2$ sweeps $[0,\tfrac32]$, **a coupling and a rigidity**. *(b)* That L121 yields a **concentration cap** $\rho^2\le\tfrac23\theta^2$ **vacuous only for $n\ge12$**: $\theta$ at a maximiser is $\max P\ge\sqrt{3/2}$ at **every** $n\ge4$, since padded $\mathbb{CP}^2$ competes at every $n$, so the cap is vacuous everywhere; substituting $\theta_0(n)$ for $\max P$ is legitimate at no $n$ and least of all below $12$, which is exactly where they differ. *(c)* That the [I12](../plan.md) slack sits in $\rho$ rather than $t_F$: **inverted**, on this family $t_F=\sum A^2=\tfrac23\Sigma$ identically while $\rho^2\equiv1$, so $t_F$ is the variable tracking $\Sigma$ and $\rho$ is the constant. *At padded $\mathbb{CP}^2$ the step $\rho\le\lVert R\rVert$ is exactly tight and all of the $2.25$ is the projection correction, as [DERIVE-mx1.md](../reviews/DERIVE-mx1.md) already records.*

> **WHAT (iii) IS WORTH, AND THE OBSTRUCTION TO USING IT.** It **characterises $W_{\mathbb{CP}^2}$** among half-flat single-four-set tensors as the unique maximiser of $\lvert\det V_+\rvert$, which is a rigidity statement the record did not hold. *Composing it with L121's $\Sigma\le\theta^2$ to exclude the rest of the family fails*: at padded $\mathbb{CP}^2$ the top eigenvector of L121's $G$ is $(A,B,C)$ itself, twice $W$'s own component in the block, the direction [Lemma L24](#lemma-l24-second-order-optimality-as-an-eigenvalue-bound-the-canonical-statement) excludes by $X\perp W$. **Equality in L121 marks the case where L121 was never derivable.**

**Validated independently before folding, sharing no code with either reviewer**: the zero-diagonal witness exists by the theorem that every traceless symmetric matrix is orthogonally similar to one with zero diagonal; and $\theta=9\det V_+$ was pinned by hand from the $SO(3)$-invariance of $P$ on traceless symmetric $3\times3$ together with the recorded $\theta=\sqrt{3/2}$ at $V_+=(2,-1,-1)/\sqrt6$.

#### Lemma L126, the four-set $\tfrac12$-threshold, met in the two-factor family only at $S^3\times S^3$

*Gate 2, [r369](../reviews/REVIEW_r369.md) and [r370](../reviews/REVIEW_r370.md), identical briefs. The threshold and its Diophantine step are confirmed by both; **the first version's reading of a $\tfrac12$ as a branch direction is withdrawn.***

**Let $M$ be a diagonal critical point **of unit norm** with $\theta>0$, which $P(M)=\theta$ requires. On the tangent space the criticality map $F(W)=Q(W)-P(W)W$ has $DF(M)=\theta(2L_e-\mathrm{Id})$, so $\ker DF(M)$ is the $\tfrac12$-eigenspace of $L_e$ (*machine-checked: `critMap_hasDerivAt` and `critMap_kernel_iff` in [L126Kernel.lean](../lean/HamiltonsCubic/Results/L126Kernel.lean)*), which by [Lemma L121](#lemma-l121-the-4-set-block-its-spectrum-and-one-necessary-condition-per-4-subset) a four-set block meets exactly when $\Sigma_{ijkl}=\theta^2$. The four-set blocks are orbit-free: the orbit tangent for the $(j,k)$ generator lies in the $\{j,k\}$ pair block, so the orbit's tangent space lies in the span of the pair blocks. *(Machine-checked: `orbit_in_pairBlock`, the pair-block form, and `orbit_perp_fourSet`, orthogonality to every four-set operator, in [L126Orbit.lean](../lean/HamiltonsCubic/Results/L126Orbit.lean).)***

**At $S^p\times S^q$, $n=p+q$, the nonzero four-set blocks are the $(2,2)$-splits, with the common value $\mu_{p,q}=\dfrac{n-2}{2(p-1)(q-1)}$, and**
$$\mu_{p,q}=\tfrac12\iff\frac1{p-1}+\frac1{q-1}=1\iff(p,q)=(3,3),$$

**the second step being $(u-1)(v-1)=1$** for $u=p-1$, $v=q-1$, which over the integers leaves $u=v=2$ alone. *Machine-checked: `L126_threshold` in [L126Sphere.lean](../lean/HamiltonsCubic/Results/L126Sphere.lean) gives $\Sigma=\theta^2\iff1/(p-1)+1/(q-1)=1$ at a unit sphere product, and [L126.lean](../lean/HamiltonsCubic/Results/L126.lean) the integer step.* *The derivation is valid at $p,q\ge2$, and $p=1$ is excluded structurally, Ricci-flatness forcing $M\equiv0$ there.* *And the value is an eigenvalue: at each $(2,2)$-split the Bianchi vector $2a-b-c$ of the four-set block is an $L_e$-eigenvector with eigenvalue $+\mu_{p,q}$, `split_eigen` and `split_value_eq` in [L126Assembly.lean](../lean/HamiltonsCubic/Results/L126Assembly.lean), and every other split's block vanishes on its Bianchi part, `nonsplit_kill` in [L126Splits.lean](../lean/HamiltonsCubic/Results/L126Splits.lean), the eigen-equation holds for `LeLin` on the bundled operators, `split_LeLin` in [L126Bridge.lean](../lean/HamiltonsCubic/Results/L126Bridge.lean), the derivative of the criticality map along it vanishes exactly at $(3,3)$, `split_DF` in [L126DF.lean](../lean/HamiltonsCubic/Results/L126DF.lean), and at a general diagonal $M$ a four-set block meets the $\tfrac12$-eigenspace of $L_e$ exactly when $\Sigma=P(M)^2$, `general_LeLin_iff` in [L126GenBridge.lean](../lean/HamiltonsCubic/Results/L126GenBridge.lean) (from `general_meets_iff` in [L126General.lean](../lean/HamiltonsCubic/Results/L126General.lean)), with `general_DF` there and `splitVec_isWeyl2` in [L126Weyl.lean](../lean/HamiltonsCubic/Results/L126Weyl.lean) placing the direction in the tangent space. All of these are composed as `L126_general`, `L126_sphere`, `L126_kernel_orbit` and `L126_block_meets_iff` in [L126Main.lean](../lean/HamiltonsCubic/Results/L126Main.lean).*

**This is exactly the pair of cases [Lemma L27](nondegeneracy.md) refuses.** That lemma is stated for $p,q\ge3$ with $(p,q)\ne(3,3)$, and L126's content is $p=2$ and $(3,3)$, **a division of labour rather than a coincidence**, and between them the two cover the whole two-factor family.

**And the threshold is met off the $(2,2)$-splits once padding enters.** A four-set carrying two indices from the first factor, one from the second and one **padded** index has $L_e$ value $\frac1{2(p-1)}$, which is exactly $\tfrac12$ at $p=2$ for every $q$, so padding moves $S^2\times S^q$ from above the threshold onto it. *Contributed at Gate 2 by [r533](../reviews/REVIEW_r533.md), and it is why the closing caveat below must stay.*
**since $u=1$ forces $\tfrac1v=0$ and $u=v=2$ is the only integer solution.**

> **A $\tfrac12$ IS A NECESSARY CONDITION FOR A BRANCH AND NOT A SUFFICIENT ONE, AND THE RECORD SETTLES THE CASE IN QUESTION.** $\ker DF(M)\ne0$ eliminates one obstruction and establishes nothing positive; orbit-freeness only says the kernel element is not a group direction. **At $S^3\times S^3$ no branch emanates along the arcs that were tested**, by [nondegeneracy.md](nondegeneracy.md)'s third-order obstruction $\Psi_1=-0.5929271$, whose constancy on the zero cone is recorded there **across 300 samples**, and whose own passage closes by leaving open the Puiseux arcs of leading exponent $\ge2$, which contribute order-three cross terms it does not exclude. *Both qualifications belong here and were dropped in transit.* *So the nine $(2,2)$-split blocks at $\tfrac12$ are a degeneracy of the second-order test, resolved against a branch at third order.*

> **WHAT THE LEMMA IS FOR, AND IT REACHES WHERE THE REPRESENTATION-THEORETIC ROUTE CANNOT.** $\mu_{2,q}=\dfrac q{2(q-1)}>\tfrac12$ at every $q$, so **no $S^2\times S^q$ is a local maximum**, its four-set block breaches [Lemma L24](#lemma-l24-second-order-optimality-as-an-eigenvalue-bound-the-canonical-statement) outright. *This rests on [Lemma L120](#lemma-l120-l_m-is-block-diagonal-over-the-sign-characters-at-every-diagonal-critical-point) and [Lemma L121](#lemma-l121-the-4-set-block-its-spectrum-and-one-necessary-condition-per-4-subset) alone, and it has to: [Lemma L27](nondegeneracy.md)'s decomposition **over-counts at $p=2$**, $86$ against $\dim\mathrm{Weyl}_6=84$ at $S^2\times S^4$, so the summand route does not reach this case ([r369](../reviews/REVIEW_r369.md)).*

**Agreement on the values.** $\mu_{2,4}=\tfrac23$ and $\mu_{2,6}=\tfrac35$ are the record's padding-invariant four-set eigenvalues, and $\mu_{2,2}=1$ matches its $(2,2)$ spectrum. *Stated at $n=p+q$: under padding the nonzero blocks are no longer only the $(2,2)$-splits.*

#### Lemma L127, the block-level refinement of [Lemma L27](nondegeneracy.md): which block each $\tfrac12$ comes from

*Gate 2, [r369](../reviews/REVIEW_r369.md) and [r370](../reviews/REVIEW_r370.md). **The first version claimed to prove sphere-product nondegeneracy for all $p,q$ and all $n$ as new. [Lemma L27](nondegeneracy.md) already does, and this refinement leans on L27's summand list for its own $\mathcal D_n$ row, so it is a refinement and not a replacement.***

**At $S^p\times S^q$ with sectional matrix $\alpha$ within the first factor, $\beta$ within the second and $\gamma$ across, the $L_e$-spectrum splits by [Lemma L120](#lemma-l120-l_m-is-block-diagonal-over-the-sign-characters-at-every-diagonal-critical-point)'s block families as:**

| block family | count | top $L_e$-eigenvalue | reaches $\tfrac12$ **exactly**? |
|---|---|---|---|
| four-set | $\binom n4$ | $\mu_{p,q}=\dfrac{n-2}{2(p-1)(q-1)}$ on the $(2,2)$-splits, $0$ otherwise | only at $(3,3)$ ([Lemma L126](#lemma-l126-the-four-set-tfrac12-threshold-met-in-the-two-factor-family-only-at-s3times-s3)) |
| pair, **cross-factor** | $pq$ | spectrum is exactly $\big\{\tfrac12\ \text{simple},\ 0^{\,n-4}\big\}$ | yes, along the orbit |
| pair, **within-factor** | $\binom p2$ and $\binom q2$, **two families** | $\dfrac{p-2}{2(p-1)}$ and $\dfrac{q-2}{2(q-1)}$ respectively, both $<\tfrac12$, **at $p,q\ge3$** | no |
| $\mathcal D_n$ | $1$ | $\sigma(L_e|_{\mathcal D_n})=\{1,\ \lambda_p^{\,p-1},\ \lambda_q^{\,q-1},\ (-\mu)^{(p-1)(q-1)},\ 0^{\text{rest}}\}$, from [Lemma L27](nondegeneracy.md)'s summands | no |

> **THE COLUMN ASKS $=\tfrac12$, AND EVERY ROW IS CORRECT UNDER THAT READING ONLY.** [Lemma L24](#lemma-l24-second-order-optimality-as-an-eigenvalue-bound-the-canonical-statement) needs $\ge\tfrac12$, and under *that* reading the four-set row's "only at $(3,3)$" is false at $\min(p,q)=2$, where $\mu_{2,q}>\tfrac12$, which [Lemma L126](#lemma-l126-the-four-set-tfrac12-threshold-met-in-the-two-factor-family-only-at-s3times-s3)'s own box states.
>
> **AND THE WITHIN-FACTOR ROW IS FALSE AT $p=2$**: the $\lambda_p$-eigenvector needs $F_1\setminus\{j,k\}$ nonempty, so at $p=2$ the block is entirely $-\mu$ and the stated top $\tfrac{p-2}{2(p-1)}=0$ is **not in the spectrum at all**, at $S^2\times S^4$ it is $(-\tfrac23)^3$ against a printed $0$. The row's conclusions survive; its value does not. *This was found by [r375](../reviews/REVIEW_r375.md) and never folded in, and the $\mathcal D_n$ row's stated source has the same gap, [r392](../reviews/REVIEW_r392.md) having recommended the repair.*
>
> **The whole within-factor spectrum, which the table does not print, is $\{\lambda_p,\ (-\mu)^{q-1},\ 0^{p-3}\}$**, and computing it is what closes the cross-check below.

**The table is forced, not merely consistent.** [Lemma L27](nondegeneracy.md) decomposes $L_e$ by $O(p)\times O(q)$ representation and this table by sign-character block family, two unrelated decompositions of one operator, and they agree on the **full spectrum with multiplicity, identically in $p,q$**, through
$$(p-1)+\binom p2=\dim S^2_0(\mathbb R^p),\qquad \binom p2\binom q2+(p-1)(q-1)+\binom p2(q-1)+\binom q2(p-1)=\dim\bigl(S^2_0\otimes S^2_0\bigr).$$
**So the $\mathcal D_n$ row is forced by L27 together with the other three rows**, rather than merely compatible with them, and $\mathop{\mathrm{tr}}L_e=0$ sums to zero off the table as well. *Gate 2, [r535](../reviews/REVIEW_r535.md) and [r536](../reviews/REVIEW_r536.md) on identical briefs, both closing the check independently.*

**Proof of the cross-factor row.** The index set $(F_1\setminus j)\cup(F_2\setminus k)$ is an equitable two-block partition, so [Lemma L122](#lemma-l122-the-pair-block-is-a-graph-laplacian-of-the-sectional-matrix)'s Laplacian has three eigenvector types on $\mathbf1^\perp$. *Sum-zero within $F_1\setminus j$*: eigenvalue $(p-1)\alpha+(q-1)\gamma$, which Ricci-flatness $(p-1)\alpha+q\gamma=0$ makes $-\gamma$, so the block value is $0$, with multiplicity $p-2$; *within $F_2\setminus k$*: identically $0$, by $(q-1)\beta+p\gamma=0$, with multiplicity $q-2$. *Constant on each part*: block value $-\tfrac12(n-1)\gamma$. Multiplicities total $n-3$. $\square$

**The third type is the orbit direction**, $x_i=M_{ki}-M_{ji}$ is $\gamma-\alpha$ on $F_1\setminus j$ and $\beta-\gamma$ on $F_2\setminus k$, so its value is $\theta/2$ and $\theta=(n-1)\lvert\gamma\rvert$. *That is $\Pi$-**saturation** at this family, which [post F40](fence-posts.md) already records, rather than the $\Pi$-bound; it is a consistency check on the computation and not a new bound. **Normalisation**: the $\tfrac12$ threshold is in $L_e$ units, L121 and L122 giving $L_M$ eigenvalues whose orbit value is $\theta/2$.*

> **WHAT THIS ADDS TO [Lemma L27](nondegeneracy.md), AND WHAT IT DOES NOT.** *Adds*: the **attribution**, each $\tfrac12$ is one cross-factor pair block, $pq$ of them, matching $\dim\mathcal O=\dim SO(n)-\binom p2-\binom q2=pq$; the exact cross-factor spectrum; and the two within-factor tops. *Does not*: establish nondegeneracy independently, since the $\mathcal D_n$ row is L27's.
>
> **AND THE CLAUSE "NO NON-DIAGONAL CRITICAL POINT BRANCHES OFF IT" IS WITHDRAWN.** $\ker DF=T_M\mathcal O$ gives tangency to the orbit and no more; a branch statement needs an equivariant slice and a Lyapunov–Schmidt reduction**, which [Lemma L128](#lemma-l128-the-branching-clause-block-checkable-nondegeneracy-at-a-pure-critical-point-and-the-isolation-it-gives) below reinstates, its part (iii) supplying what was missing and needing neither**, and a branch tangent to a $\mathcal D_n$ direction can leave the diagonal at second order. *The record already says this in its own words: the obstruction proves no branch when it fails, rather than a branch when it vanishes.*

**Agreement**: the arithmetic reproduces the record's $\dim E(\tfrac12)=18$ at $S^3\times S^3$, nine cross-factor pair blocks against the nine-dimensional orbit, plus nine four-set blocks, and its six orbit-free within-factor blocks among fifteen.

#### What L128 leaves owed at a pure critical point, restated

*Migrated from [I18](../plan.md) on 2026-09-03, where these two clauses were the record's only copy. Both come from the Gate 2 restatement of 2026-09-02.*

> **THE THRESHOLD IS CLOSED, $\theta\ge\sqrt{3/2}$**, because [Lemma L128](#lemma-l128-the-branching-clause-block-checkable-nondegeneracy-at-a-pure-critical-point-and-the-isolation-it-gives)(iv)'s limit point carries it. *The alternative discharge is to prove that no pure critical point sits at $\sqrt{3/2}$ exactly.*

> **THE $\mathcal D_n$ BLOCK IS A LIVE THIRD CLAUSE.** It is covered by neither the two-block sphere products nor the general pure point, because **the diagonal grant is a value statement while this is a second-order one**, and degenerate diagonal points are already on the record, [Lemma L11](nondegeneracy.md)'s plateau and the $\mathfrak{su}(m)$ family, with kernel excesses to $20$, all at $\theta\le1.054$.

#### Lemma L170, at a local maximum the $\tfrac12$-eigenspace is the Hessian kernel, so [L13](curvature-invariants.md)'s clause is Morse–Bott nondegeneracy and not a theorem

*Gate 2, [r751](../reviews/REVIEW_r751.md) and [r752](../reviews/REVIEW_r752.md), on identical briefs, **both `PROVEN, hypotheses corrected`** and agreeing that the local-maximum hypothesis is unused. The equality is wider than the block first claimed and two of its surrounding statements were wrong.*

> **A NEW RESULT THE AUDIT DERIVED EN ROUTE.** *At a local maximum $P$ vanishes identically on $\ker\mathrm{Hess}$*, from the $t^3$ term along a great circle, **the record's own cubic-order mechanism at $S^3\times S^3$, recovered independently**, and a one-value test that would discharge the remaining non-maximality debt.

**Statement.** Let $W$ be a critical point of $P|_S$ at unit norm with $\theta\ne0$, and let $E(\tfrac12)$ be the $\tfrac12$-eigenspace of $L_e$. Then

$$E(\tfrac12)=\ker\mathrm{Hess}(P|_S)_W,\qquad\text{so}\qquad E(\tfrac12)=T_W\mathcal O\iff W\ \text{is Morse–Bott nondegenerate.}$$

> **MAXIMALITY IS NOT A HYPOTHESIS, AND AN EARLIER VERSION OF THIS BLOCK MADE IT ONE.** *Both audits refuted it independently: $L_e$ preserves $W^\perp$ and is self-adjoint there, so $\ker\mathrm{Hess}$ is the zero-eigenspace of $L_e-\tfrac12$ **whatever the sign of the form**, and only $\theta\ne0$ is used.* **The wider statement is what [L128](#lemma-l128-the-branching-clause-block-checkable-nondegeneracy-at-a-pure-critical-point-and-the-isolation-it-gives)(ii) already proves in passing**, and it is what claim (a)'s own inference at two non-maxima requires.

**Hence [L13](curvature-invariants.md)'s clause is a nondegeneracy hypothesis rather than a statement awaiting proof**, and at a pure $W$ it is decided by [Lemma L128](#lemma-l128-the-branching-clause-block-checkable-nondegeneracy-at-a-pure-critical-point-and-the-isolation-it-gives)'s block criterion.

**Proof.** $\mathrm{Hess}(P|_S)_W=6\theta(L_e-\tfrac12\mathrm{Id})$ on $W^\perp$, the constant $6\theta$ is forced by the cubic Taylor expansion rather than chosen, and **only its non-vanishing is used**. $L_e$ preserves $W^\perp$ and is self-adjoint there, so $\ker\mathrm{Hess}=E(\tfrac12)\cap W^\perp$. **The intersection is the whole eigenspace**: $L_eW=W$, which needs $L_e$ self-adjoint, from the total symmetry of the cubic's polarisation, so $E(\tfrac12)\perp W$. Morse–Bott nondegeneracy is $\ker\mathrm{Hess}=T_W\mathcal O$ in the record's sense, *nondegeneracy relative to the orbit*; the general notion carries more, and the surplus is [L128](#lemma-l128-the-branching-clause-block-checkable-nondegeneracy-at-a-pure-critical-point-and-the-isolation-it-gives)(iii) rather than a definition. $\square$

> **WHY THE TWO WITNESSES DO NOT REFUTE THE CLAUSE.** *$S^3\times S^3$ gives $18$ against an orbit of $9$ and $S^2\times S^3\times S^3$ gives $60$ against $21$, both degenerate.* **$\mu_{3,3}=\tfrac12$ on nine four-set blocks is the first, exactly**; for the second an exact four-set block at $L_e$-value $\tfrac34>\tfrac12$ breaches [L24](#lemma-l24-second-order-optimality-as-an-eigenvalue-bound-the-canonical-statement) and so rules out maximality analytically. *That the first is not a local maximum rests on a third-order computation in [nondegeneracy.md](nondegeneracy.md) with no closed form on file, one audit called it confirmed and analytic, the other hypothesis-debt, and the debt is the second block's alone.*

> **AND "NO PROOF IS AVAILABLE" IS OVERSTATED; THE DEFENSIBLE FORM IS PROVED.** *An exact cubic witness closes each of analyticity, compactness, equivariance and cubic homogeneity with a reason*, **none of them can force nondegeneracy at a maximum, even a global one.* The clause remains a hypothesis, and the record's downstream use, which consumes "at least" alone, is already the right strength.*
