> **AT A MAXIMUM [L128](#lemma-l128-the-branching-clause-block-checkable-nondegeneracy-at-a-pure-critical-point-and-the-isolation-it-gives)(ii)'S EXCLUSION READS AS A STRICT INEQUALITY, AND THAT BUYS NOTHING.** *Four-set blocks lie in $W^\perp$, exactly, by sign-character orthogonality, which is what purity buys rather than a generic position, and there $L_e\preceq\tfrac12$ by [L24](#lemma-l24-second-order-optimality-as-an-eigenvalue-bound-the-canonical-statement), so excluding $\tfrac12$ and asserting $\mu<\tfrac12$ are the same statement.* **The $\le$ half is free and the non-equality is what still has to be proved**, so nothing is saved. *The same simplification reaches $\mathcal D_n\cap W^\perp$ and every orbit-free pair block, which the first version understated.*

> **AND IT DOES NOT REACH [I18](../plan.md), WHICH AN EARLIER VERSION CLAIMED.** *That item quantifies over pure **critical** points above $\sqrt{3/2}$, not over maxima, so the repair weakening its obligation from $\Sigma<\theta^2$ to $\Sigma\ne\theta^2$ stands unaffected.* **A scope error, refuted at audit.**

#### Lemma L128, the branching clause: block-checkable nondegeneracy at a pure critical point, and the isolation it gives

*Derived 2026-09-02 as [I22](../plan.md) on route [R16](routes.md). **Gate 2, [r373](../reviews/REVIEW_r373.md) and [r374](../reviews/REVIEW_r374.md) on identical briefs, agreeing on every verdict.** The first version stated (ii) with strict inequalities and proved (iii) through an affine slice; **both are corrected here, each by two independent readings**, and the novelty paragraph is corrected in the direction that costs the lemma its claim.*

**Let $W$ be a pure (frame-diagonal) critical point of unit norm with $\theta=P(W)>0$ and sectional matrix $M$, in a fixed frame. Write $\mathcal O=SO(n)\cdot W$ and decompose $\mathcal W_n$ by [Lemma L120](#lemma-l120-l_m-is-block-diagonal-over-the-sign-characters-at-every-diagonal-critical-point). Then:**

> **(i) (Location of the orbit, on record, restated for use.) $T_W\mathcal O\subseteq\bigoplus_{\{j,k\}}V_{jk}$, and the $\{j,k\}$-component of $[\rho(E_{jk}),W]$ is $o^{jk}_i=M_{ki}-M_{ji}$, $i\notin\{j,k\}$. The four-set blocks and $\mathcal D_n$ carry no orbit direction.**
>
> **(ii) (Block criterion.) $W$ is Morse–Bott nondegenerate, $\ker\mathrm{Hess}(P|_S)_W=T_W\mathcal O$, if and only if $\tfrac12$ is NOT an $L_e$-eigenvalue of any four-set block or of $\mathcal D_n\cap W^\perp$, and in every pair block $\tfrac12$ is either absent or simple with eigenvector $o^{jk}$.**
>
> **(iii) (Branching clause.) If (ii) holds then some neighbourhood of $\mathcal O$ in $S(\mathcal W_n)$ contains no critical point outside $\mathcal O$. Hence a critical point outside $\mathcal O$ can approach $\mathcal O$ only if (ii) fails, only where a non-orbit block carries $\tfrac12$ exactly.**
>
> **(iv) (Uniform separation, the form [I5](../plan.md) consumes.) If (ii) holds at every pure critical point with $\theta\ge c>0$, then $A=\{\text{non-pure critical}\}\cap\{\theta\ge c\}$ and $B=O(n)\cdot(\mathcal D_n\cap S)$ have $\overline A\cap B=\varnothing$, and both are compact, so $d(A,B)>0$.**

> **THE INEQUALITIES IN (ii) ARE EXCLUSIONS OF $\tfrac12$, NOT STRICT BOUNDS, AND THE DIFFERENCE HAS A WITNESS ON THE RECORD.** *The first version wrote "$L_e$-spectrum $<\tfrac12$" and both reviewers refuted it with the same example.* A block **above** $\tfrac12$ is possible because the hypothesis is criticality, not maximality: $S^2\times S^4$ at $n=6$ is pure, critical and Morse–Bott nondegenerate ([Lemma L11](nondegeneracy.md)), while its $(2,2)$ four-set blocks sit at $\mu_{2,4}=\tfrac23>\tfrac12$, a value [Lemma L121](#lemma-l121-the-4-set-block-its-spectrum-and-one-necessary-condition-per-4-subset) prints by name. **[I18](../plan.md) inherits the repair**: what it must prove per four-subset is $\Sigma\ne\theta^2$, not $\Sigma<\theta^2$, a strictly weaker and sufficient statement.

**Proof of (i).** $\rho(E_{jk})$ fixes $e_{jk}$ and every $e_{ab}$ with $a,b\notin\{j,k\}$ and joins $e_{ji}$ to $e_{ki}$ for $i\notin\{j,k\}$, with the sign convention that makes the commutator's coefficient $M_{ki}-M_{ji}$. Since $We_{ab}=M_{ab}e_{ab}$, the entries of $[\rho(E_{jk}),W]$ are $(M_\beta-M_\alpha)\rho(E_{jk})_{\alpha\beta}$, so it is supported on the span of $f_i=e_{ji}\odot e_{ki}$, which is [Lemma L122](#lemma-l122-the-pair-block-is-a-graph-laplacian-of-the-sectional-matrix)'s basis of $V_{jk}$. Linearity over $A=\sum a_{jk}E_{jk}$ puts each summand in its own pair block. $\square$ *Both halves are on record, [DERIVE-df1](../reviews/DERIVE-df1.md) §DF1b step 4(ii) in bold with this argument, [Lemma L126](#lemma-l126-the-four-set-tfrac12-threshold-met-in-the-two-factor-family-only-at-s3times-s3) inside its own statement, and [r370](../reviews/REVIEW_r370.md)'s verdict Y2, and it is restated here only because (ii) reads off it.*

**Proof of (ii).** [Lemma L24](#lemma-l24-second-order-optimality-as-an-eigenvalue-bound-the-canonical-statement) gives $\mathrm{Hess}(P|_S)_W=6\theta(L_e-\tfrac12\mathrm{Id})$ on $T_WS=W^\perp$, so $\ker\mathrm{Hess}=E_{L_e}(\tfrac12)\cap W^\perp$, an eigenspace, which is why the criterion excludes the value $\tfrac12$ rather than bounding the spectrum. [Lemma L120](#lemma-l120-l_m-is-block-diagonal-over-the-sign-characters-at-every-diagonal-critical-point) makes that eigenspace the sum of its block components; [Lemma L122](#lemma-l122-the-pair-block-is-a-graph-laplacian-of-the-sectional-matrix) puts each $o^{jk}$ in it. So $\ker\mathrm{Hess}=T_W\mathcal O$ says exactly that no block contributes a $\tfrac12$-vector beyond the $o^{jk}$. $\square$

**Proof of (iii), in the ambient space and needing no slice.** Let $F(X)=3\big(Q(X)-P(X)X\big)$ on $\mathcal W_n$, so that $\mathrm{Crit}=F^{-1}(0)\cap S$ and, near $W$ where $P>0$, $F^{-1}(0)$ is $\mathcal O$ itself. *$Q$ preserves $\mathcal W_n$, [Böhm–Wilking](../references/boehm-wilking-2008-manifolds-with-positive-curvature-operators/notes.md) eq. (6), so $F$ is the field written.* Then $dF_W$ is self-adjoint, sends $W\mapsto-6\theta W$, and restricts to $6\theta(L_e-\tfrac12)$ on $W^\perp$, so under (ii) $\ker dF_W=T_W\mathcal O$ **in the ambient space**. Split $\mathcal W_n=T_W\mathcal O\oplus V'$ and apply the implicit function theorem to $\Pi_{V'}F=0$: $\Pi_{V'}dF_W|_{V'}$ is invertible, self-adjointness gives $\mathop{\mathrm{im}}dF_W\subseteq(\ker dF_W)^\perp=V'$, hence $\Pi_{V'}dF_W|_{V'}=dF_W|_{V'}$, so $F^{-1}(0)$ lies locally in a graph of dimension $\dim\mathcal O$. $\mathcal O$ is a submanifold of that dimension inside $F^{-1}(0)$ tangent to $T_W\mathcal O$, so it *is* the graph near $W$. Equivariance transports this to every $gW$, and $\mathcal O$ is compact, so finitely many such neighbourhoods cover it. $\square$

**Proof of (iv).** $B$ is the continuous image of the compact $O(n)\times(\mathcal D_n\cap S)$, hence compact; $\overline A$ is closed in the compact $S$, hence compact, **and it must be the closure**, since $A$ is cut by the *open* complement of $B$, so nothing elementary closes $A$ itself and \"$A$ is closed\" is equivalent to the conclusion being proved. The step is not needed: disjoint compacts give $d(A,B)\ge d(\overline A,B)>0$. A point of $\overline A\cap B$ is critical ($\mathrm{Crit}$ is closed), pure, and has $\theta\ge c$ by continuity, so (iii) applies at it and its neighbourhood contains no non-pure critical point, contradicting membership in $\overline A$. Hence $\overline A\cap B=\varnothing$ and $d(A,B)>0$. $\square$ **The threshold must be closed**: $\theta\ge c$, since $\overline A$ is reached by a limit. *[I18](../plan.md) is stated at $\theta>\sqrt{3/2}$ and must be restated at $\theta\ge\sqrt{3/2}$ for this bridge to close.*

**What is assembly and what is new, corrected at Gate 2.** *On record and used as given*: the Hessian normalisation ([Lemma L24](#lemma-l24-second-order-optimality-as-an-eigenvalue-bound-the-canonical-statement)); the decomposition and block-diagonality ([Lemma L120](#lemma-l120-l_m-is-block-diagonal-over-the-sign-characters-at-every-diagonal-critical-point)); the pair-block form and $L_eo^{jk}=\tfrac12o^{jk}$ ([Lemma L122](#lemma-l122-the-pair-block-is-a-graph-laplacian-of-the-sectional-matrix)); **the whole of (i), both halves** ([DERIVE-df1](../reviews/DERIVE-df1.md), [Lemma L126](#lemma-l126-the-four-set-tfrac12-threshold-met-in-the-two-factor-family-only-at-s3times-s3), [r370](../reviews/REVIEW_r370.md)), *the first version claimed its negative half as new and both reviewers found it printed three times, once ten lines above in this file*. *New*: the criterion (ii) at an arbitrary pure critical point, where [Lemma L127](#lemma-l127-the-block-level-refinement-of-lemma-l27-which-block-each-tfrac12-comes-from) has the block table only at sphere products; the proof of (iii), which [Lemma L11](nondegeneracy.md) asserts without argument; and (iv).

> **AND IT DOES REINSTATE [Lemma L127](#lemma-l127-the-block-level-refinement-of-lemma-l27-which-block-each-tfrac12-comes-from)'S WITHDRAWN CLAUSE, IN STRONGER FORM.** *The first version's box claimed the opposite and both reviewers refuted it.* $\ker DF=T_M\mathcal O$ **is** Morse–Bott nondegeneracy, since $\mathrm{Hess}=3\,DF$, so the hypotheses are identical and there is no distinction to draw. The clause was withdrawn for want of an equivariant slice and a Lyapunov–Schmidt reduction; (iii) supplies what was missing and needs neither. What stays withdrawn is the reading that made the clause unconditional: at $S^3\times S^3$, where nine four-set blocks sit at exactly $\tfrac12$, (ii) fails and (iii) says nothing, correctly, since that is the one two-block sphere product [Lemma L11](nondegeneracy.md) excepts.

**Scope.** **The decomposition itself carries no dimension restriction**, [Lemma L120](#lemma-l120-l_m-is-block-diagonal-over-the-sign-characters-at-every-diagonal-critical-point) proves it over the full sign group at every $n$, and only the determinant-one subgroup needs the $n=4,6,8$ exclusions. $Q(\mathcal W_n)\subseteq\mathcal W_n$ (Böhm–Wilking, above); $\theta>0$, inherited from [Lemma L24](#lemma-l24-second-order-optimality-as-an-eigenvalue-bound-the-canonical-statement) and load-bearing there; Ricci-flatness, through [Lemma L122](#lemma-l122-the-pair-block-is-a-graph-laplacian-of-the-sectional-matrix)'s identification of the Ricci cut with $\mathbf 1^\perp$; **one fixed eigenframe**, since the decomposition is the sign group's of that frame; and the orbit is $SO(n)\cdot W$ while the decomposing group is the full sign group of [Lemma L120](#lemma-l120-l_m-is-block-diagonal-over-the-sign-characters-at-every-diagonal-critical-point), harmless at $n\ge9$ where nothing fuses.

**Corollary L128.1, every two-block sphere product with $p,q\ge3$ at $n\ge12$ satisfies the criterion, hence is isolated.** *Assembly of recorded block data; the isolation is what is new, and it needs (iii).* **Assume $p,q\ge3$**, which is where [Lemma L27](nondegeneracy.md)'s summand list is stated and where its decomposition counts correctly, *at $p=2$ it over-counts, $86$ against $84$ at $S^2\times S^4$, as [Lemma L126](#lemma-l126-the-four-set-tfrac12-threshold-met-in-the-two-factor-family-only-at-s3times-s3)'s own box records; both Gate 2 reviewers flagged the omission ([r375](../reviews/REVIEW_r375.md), [r376](../reviews/REVIEW_r376.md)).* **The gap is empty in this route's range**: every sphere product clearing $\sqrt{3/2}$ at $n\ge12$ has $\min(p,q)\ge5$, since $p=2$ tops out below $1$, $p=3$ at $2/\sqrt3$ and $p=4$ approaches $\sqrt{3/2}$ from below at every $n$ ([r376](../reviews/REVIEW_r376.md)). Check the three clauses at $S^p\times S^q$, $p+q=n\ge12$: the **four-set** blocks meet $\tfrac12$ exactly when $\Sigma=\theta^2$, which [Lemma L126](#lemma-l126-the-four-set-tfrac12-threshold-met-in-the-two-factor-family-only-at-s3times-s3) shows happens iff $\tfrac1{p-1}+\tfrac1{q-1}=1$, i.e. only at $S^3\times S^3$ and so never at $n\ge12$; the **pair** blocks are $\{\tfrac12\text{ simple},0^{n-4}\}$ across factors with the $\tfrac12$ *on the orbit*, and top out at $(p-2)/(2(p-1))<\tfrac12$ within a factor ([Lemma L127](#lemma-l127-the-block-level-refinement-of-lemma-l27-which-block-each-tfrac12-comes-from)); and $\mathcal D_n\cap W^\perp$ carries $\{\lambda_p,\lambda_q,-\mu,0\}$ from [Lemma L27](nondegeneracy.md)'s summands, all $\ne\tfrac12$ by the same two inequalities. *A cleaner argument for that leg, from [r375](../reviews/REVIEW_r375.md): L27's only $\tfrac12$-summand is $\mathbb R^p\otimes\mathbb R^q=T_W\mathcal O$, which part (i) places in the pair blocks and therefore outside $\mathcal D_n$.* So (ii) holds, and (iii) isolates the orbit. **This settles [I18](../plan.md) sub-step (b) outright** and leaves the item's weight entirely on the arbitrary pure critical point.

**Where it is used.** [I18](../plan.md) asks whether (ii) holds at every pure critical point with $\theta\ge\sqrt{3/2}$ at $n\ge12$: by [Lemma L121](#lemma-l121-the-4-set-block-its-spectrum-and-one-necessary-condition-per-4-subset) the four-set half is $\Sigma\ne\theta^2$ at every four-subset, and by [Lemma L122](#lemma-l122-the-pair-block-is-a-graph-laplacian-of-the-sectional-matrix) the pair half is a simplicity statement about the graph Laplacians $\Delta^{(jk)}$. **The failure mode is a named object rather than a gap**: a pure critical point above the threshold with a four-set at exactly $\Sigma=\theta^2$ is degenerate by (ii), which is the branch point the route is looking for.

## Post F46, the half-flat single-four-set family carries exactly one critical point, so it can supply no exclusion theorem

*[r367](../reviews/REVIEW_r367.md) and [r368](../reviews/REVIEW_r368.md), identical briefs, agreeing. **The first version of this post argued the closure from a slack factor and got that factor wrong; the argument is replaced.***

> **THE FAMILY CONTAINS ONE CRITICAL POINT, NAMELY $\pm W_{\mathbb{CP}^2}$.** On the half-flat cone $\theta=9\det V_+$, and $\nabla\det$ on the sphere of traceless symmetric $3\times3$ forces two equal eigenvalues, hence $V_+\propto(2,-1,-1)$. **So [Lemma L24](#lemma-l24-second-order-optimality-as-an-eigenvalue-bound-the-canonical-statement) cannot be evaluated at any other member**, there is nothing in the family for an exclusion theorem to exclude, and a route seeking one is closed for that reason rather than by any weakness of the bound.

**What a successor must clear.** A family with more than one critical point in it. *And it must not reach for [Lemma L124](#lemma-l124-on-the-half-flat-four-set-family-theta9det-v_-and-theta2letfrac32rho2-with-equality-exactly-at-pm-w_mathbbcp2)(iii) as an ordering of $\theta$ against $\Sigma$: that holds only in $V_+$'s eigenframe, $\Sigma$ being frame-dependent.*

**Two constants, both from Gate 2.** *(i)* **The slack factor is $\tfrac94$, not $4$.** On the half-flat family exactly half of $\lVert W\rVert^2$ is the trivial-character diagonal and half the four-set block, so $c^2\le\tfrac12$ with equality at $\mathbb{CP}^2$, giving $(1+c^2)^2=2.25$, **which is the figure [DERIVE-mx1.md](../reviews/DERIVE-mx1.md) already prints and which two paragraphs of [Lemma L124](#lemma-l124-on-the-half-flat-four-set-family-theta9det-v_-and-theta2letfrac32rho2-with-equality-exactly-at-pm-w_mathbbcp2) already record.** *The error was substituting the within-block alignment cosine, which is $1$, for the full-space overlap, which is $1/\sqrt2$.* *(ii)* **"Consistent for every member, so no member is excluded" was false**: with $c^2\le\tfrac12$ the chain forces $\theta^2\ge\tfrac23$, which does exclude part of the family, a moot point given the box above, but the sentence asserted the opposite of what its own inequality gives.

> **AND ITS STATED OPEN PROBLEM WAS ALREADY SOLVED ON THE RECORD.** "A bound on $c$ itself at a maximiser" is [DERIVE-mx1.md](../reviews/DERIVE-mx1.md)'s $\sum_Sc_S^2\le\lVert R\rVert^2=1-t_F/2$, with its corollary that at most $\lfloor1/t\rfloor$ of the $\binom n4$ subsets have $c^2>t$. *The post named as owed a bound the route item it belongs to was already carrying.*

## Post F44, the second-order criterion on single-character directions is blind to the off-diagonal part

**[Lemma L123](#lemma-l123-a-zero-diagonal-operator-contributes-nothing-to-a-single-character-rayleigh-quotient) is a limitation as well as a tool.** It says that for $X$ in a single sign-character space and $R$ with zero diagonal, $\langle X,R\circ X\rangle=0$. Read the other way round:

> **THE BLINDNESS TAKES A SHARPER FORM.** *[r357](../reviews/REVIEW_r357.md) and [r358](../reviews/REVIEW_r358.md), independently.* **$R$ reaches the criterion through two channels the first statement missed**: the bound itself is $\kappa=\theta/2$ and $\theta$ depends on all of $W$, [Lemma L121](#lemma-l121-the-4-set-block-its-spectrum-and-one-necessary-condition-per-4-subset)'s own $\le\theta^2$ shows it in print, and **which directions are admissible is $R$'s doing**, since in a non-trivial character space $\langle X,W\rangle=\langle X,R\rangle$. *The first version conceded the second channel two paragraphs below its own box, which is a self-contradiction rather than a scope note.*

> **THE REPLACEMENT, proved and screened** ([r358](../reviews/REVIEW_r358.md), zero-tests at $4\times10^{-16}$, off-block $L_R$ up to $2.14$ at $n=6$): **$L_{\mathop{\mathrm{diag}}(M)}$ is exactly the character-block-diagonal part of $L_W$, and $L_R$ exactly the off-block part.** So $R$ is **off-block, not invisible**, a single-character *Rayleigh quotient* cannot see it, but $L_R$ moves that direction into other blocks and is perfectly visible to anything reading across blocks.

> **AND THE BLINDNESS IS PER-FRAME, WHICH REOPENS A ROUTE THE POST CLOSED.** The family $\{\mathop{\mathrm{diag}}(M_F)\}$ over *all* frames $F$ is the full sectional-curvature function, which **determines $W$** ([r357](../reviews/REVIEW_r357.md)). So "a successor must mix characters or leave Rayleigh quotients" omits a third option: **vary the frame**. That is what [DERIVE-mx1.md](../reviews/DERIVE-mx1.md) does.

**Why the per-frame statement still bites.** The natural test directions are exactly the single-character ones, the $4$-set planes, the pair blocks, the diagonal locus, because those are where [Lemma L120](#lemma-l120-l_m-is-block-diagonal-over-the-sign-characters-at-every-diagonal-critical-point)'s block decomposition makes $L_M$ computable in closed form ([Lemma L121](#lemma-l121-the-4-set-block-its-spectrum-and-one-necessary-condition-per-4-subset), [Lemma L122](#lemma-l122-the-pair-block-is-a-graph-laplacian-of-the-sectional-matrix)). **The very structure that makes the criterion evaluable is what makes it blind**, and a test built from those blocks measures the frame-diagonal part however non-diagonal $W$ is.

**Two consequences already visible on the record.** The four-index certificate is a condition on $M$ in every frame ([DERIVE-mx1.md](../reviews/DERIVE-mx1.md)), that is this post stated positively, and it is why the certificate's only $R$-dependence enters through the *projection* defect $\langle X,W\rangle$ rather than through $L_R$. And the closed forms of L121 and L122 hold under diagonality alone, which is the same fact again: they never see $R$ because they cannot.

**What is not closed.** This says nothing about criteria that are not Rayleigh quotients on a single character space, the third-order fusion conditions, the $\Delta^{(jk)}$ Laplacian read across pairs, or any test using a direction with components in two characters. *The post is about a method, not about the problem.*

## Post F47, no lossy estimate can prove the pair clause, because in a non-orbit-free block it is an exact equality

*[r381](../reviews/REVIEW_r381.md) and [r382](../reviews/REVIEW_r382.md), identical briefs, agreeing; each found it while refuting a different bound, and neither was asked for a fence.*

**The fact.** At every pure critical point and every pair $\{j,k\}$, $B(u-v)=0$, [r378](../reviews/REVIEW_r378.md)'s relation, independent of maximality, so with $\widetilde N=-\Delta^{(jk)}$ and $T=\theta+M_{jk}$,
$$\widetilde N\,o=T\,o,\qquad o_i=M_{ki}-M_{ji}.$$
**Hence $\lambda_{\max}(\widetilde N)\ge T$ whenever $o\ne0$, and the pair clause there is not an inequality with slack but the statement that $T$ is EXACTLY the top eigenvalue.**

**What it fences.** Any argument that bounds $\lambda_{\max}(\widetilde N)$ by something strictly larger than the truth, a Gershgorin envelope, a moment ceiling, a subadditive split, a joint quadratic-form estimate, **cannot certify an equality**, so it can never establish the clause in a block with $o\ne0$. *Four attempts died here before the pattern was named: the row-mass condition, the trace–Frobenius ceiling, the rank-one split and the joint estimate, the last two after the mechanism had already been recorded against the first two and not carried forward.*

**Corrected at Gate 2 ([r384](../reviews/REVIEW_r384.md)): the exceptional blocks are the MAJORITY, not the minority.** The $664/664$ figure censused **cross pairs**, where $o\ne0$ holds by construction. Over *all* pairs the orbit-free population is $30/66$ at $S^6\times S^6$, $31/66$ at $S^5\times S^7$, the only two in-band points at $n=12$, and $605/780$ at $S^5\times S^{35}$: **$45\%$ to $78\%$**. *The fence stands as a statement about which blocks an estimate can reach; the claim that they were few does not.* **And the $o\ne0$ blocks are now closed by the ordering theorem** ([ATTEMPTS.md](../ATTEMPTS.md)), so what this post fences is exactly the set that remains open.

**Orbit-free pairs are non-generic:** at $S^6\times S^6$, $36$ of the $66$ pairs are cross pairs, and over every in-band $S^p\times S^q$ with $p,q\le40$ the count is $664$ cross pairs, all of them equality blocks. **A method restricted to $o=0$ therefore has a generically empty domain**, and saying so is part of proposing one.

**What a successor must do instead.** Prove that the orbit direction *is* the top of the spectrum, an eigenvector-ordering statement, not a bound. The record's own [Lemma L24](second-order.md) delivers exactly that at a **maximiser**, which is why the clause is unproblematic there; [I18](../plan.md) needs it at a **non-maximal** critical point in the band, and that is the whole content of the item. *A Perron-type argument, a variational characterisation of $o$, or an interlacing that pins $\lambda_2$ would each be of the right kind; an estimate of any sharpness would not.*

## Post F48, $\lambda_{\max}(\widetilde N)\le\theta+M_{jk}$ is FALSE at padded pairs, and it was never what the pair clause asked

*[r385](../reviews/REVIEW_r385.md), with [r386](../reviews/REVIEW_r386.md) agreeing on the surrounding corrections. It closes a target that five successive attempts in [ATTEMPTS.md](../ATTEMPTS.md) were aimed at.*

**The witness.** Pad the $S^6\times S^6$ critical point with two zero rows to $n=14$. Every hypothesis survives, $M$ hollow and symmetric, $M\mathbf1=0$, $\lVert M\rVert_F^2=2$, and criticality at a padded index reads $0+0=\theta\cdot0$, and $\theta$ is unchanged, so the point is in the band. For the pair of padded indices $u=v=0$, so the pair is a **twin**, $M_{jk}=0$, $T=\theta$, and $\widetilde N=M'$ is the original matrix. Then $\mathop{\mathrm{tr}}M'=0$ with $M'\ne0$ forces $\lambda_{\max}(M')>0$, and the recorded gap $\mathop{\mathrm{spec}}(M)\cap(0,\theta)=\varnothing$ forces $\lambda_{\max}(M')\ge\theta=T$, **strictly at every sphere product, where $\lambda_1>\theta$.**

**Why no sharpening rescues it.** The trace bound is **exactly tight** at this point, so the failure is not slack in an estimate: the inequality being estimated is false. *Any instrument that proves $\lambda_{\max}(\widetilde N)\le T$ in general is therefore proving something untrue, and the search for one is closed.*

**What the pair clause actually asks, and it survives.** [Lemma L128](second-order.md)(ii) excludes the **value** $\tfrac12$ rather than bounding the spectrum, so through [Lemma L122](second-order.md) the clause is
$$T\notin\mathop{\mathrm{spec}}\big(\widetilde N|_{\mathbf1^\perp}\big)\ \text{off the orbit direction} .$$
**That is a separation statement, and it holds at the padded pair** where the bound fails. *The five attempts recorded in [ATTEMPTS.md](../ATTEMPTS.md), the row-mass condition, the trace–Frobenius ceiling, the rank-one split, the joint estimate and the trace bound on $\mathbf1^\perp$, were all aimed at the stronger statement.*

**The two ways forward, and they are the only two.** *(i)* **Prove the separation directly**, abandoning $\lambda_{\max}\le T$; no moment argument reaches it, since at the padded pair $T$ sits strictly inside the spectrum's convex hull. *(ii)* **Add the hypothesis that excludes padding**, no zero row, equivalently $s_i>0$ for every $i$, equivalently $\circ$-indecomposability ([support-dichotomy.md](support-dichotomy.md)), under which $\lambda_{\max}\le T$ is at least not known to be false. **That hypothesis is free at a maximiser and is exactly what a maximiser has and a critical point does not**, which is the seam [r376](../reviews/REVIEW_r376.md) identified when it first split this clause.

## Post F49, a condition $\langle(\widetilde N-T)y,x\rangle=0$ can never contribute to the separation, for any $y$

*[r388](../reviews/REVIEW_r388.md), with [r387](../reviews/REVIEW_r387.md) reaching the same conclusion by counting: the record supplies $n-1$ such conditions, not three, and all of them are inert.*

**The fact.** For a symmetric operator, $\ker(\widetilde N-T)\perp\mathop{\mathrm{ran}}(\widetilde N-T)$. So $\langle(\widetilde N-T)y,\ x\rangle=0$ holds for **every** $y$ and every $x$ in the $T$-eigenspace. **A collection of such conditions is therefore satisfiable by construction and can never be inconsistent**, it constrains where a $T$-eigenvector lies, never whether one exists.

**What it fences.** The three constraints opened in [ATTEMPTS.md](../ATTEMPTS.md) are all of this form: Constraint 1 is $y=u$ (since $u\circ u-2M_{jk}u=(\widetilde N-T)u$ at a twin pair), Constraint 3 is $y=\Pi w$ and says so, and even $x\perp\mathbf1$ is $y=\mathbf1$, since $(\widetilde N-T)\mathbf1=-T\mathbf1$. **The stated next step, closing them against $\lVert x\rVert=1$ and $\langle\mathbf1,x\rangle=0$, cannot reach a contradiction.** *And the chain's supposed obstruction dies with it: $u\circ u\in\mathcal K(\widetilde N,u)$, so $\widetilde N(u\circ u)$ merely restates $\widetilde N^2u$, which quadratic criticality cannot reach and which would be inert if it could.*

**What is left standing, and it is one instrument.** [r388](../reviews/REVIEW_r388.md)'s Constraint 2 is **not** of this form, it comes from $M^2-\theta M=L(w)\succeq0$ rather than from the eigen-equation, and in its exact form it is an identity rather than an inequality,
$$T M_{jk}-(2\theta+4M_{jk})S_1+2S_2+2c^2\;=\;R\;\ge\;0,$$
$R$ the Dirichlet energy on the $N$-graph. *The version recorded first discarded $2S_2$, and that term is the decisive one: at a padded pair the discarded form reads $0\ge0$ while the identity forces $x$ constant on components, hence $x=0$, an independent second proof of [Lemma L129](diagonal-half.md)'s corollary.* **A successor works with the Dirichlet identity, or with something outside the eigen-equation entirely.**

#### Lemma L133, the balanced-orbit stability threshold in closed form, and it clears $\kappa_n$ for every $n\ge12$

*Derived 2026-09-03 as [I2](../plan.md) on route [R17](../routes.md), the first of the three scenario constants of [Conjecture L16](problem.md)'s stability formulation. **Gate 2 LANDED, [r419](../reviews/REVIEW_r419.md) and [r420](../reviews/REVIEW_r420.md) on identical briefs, both CONFIRMED, each having recomputed the spectrum, the Daleckii–Krein coefficients, the support table and $\mu$ from the definitions before reading the derivation.** Their corrections are applied here: the margin ran the wrong way, the maximiser set was stated too narrowly, the granted diagonal case is **not** needed, and the [Corollary L128.1](#lemma-l128-the-branching-clause-block-checkable-nondegeneracy-at-a-pure-critical-point-and-the-isolation-it-gives) citation was a category error. [r420](../reviews/REVIEW_r420.md) pinned the norm convention independently by recomputing $\delta^2(W_{\mathbb{CP}^2})=\tfrac19$ by hand.*

**Setting.** $n=2m\ge12$; $W^*$ the unit-norm Weyl tensor of $S^m\times S^m$, a pure critical point with $\theta=P(W^*)=\theta_0(n)=\sqrt{2(n-1)(n-2)}/n$; $e=W^*/\theta_0$; $\delta^2(W)=\lVert\mathrm{Alt}(W_-)\rVert^2$ the defect of [Conjecture L16](problem.md), $W_-$ the negative spectral part of $W$ as an operator on $\Lambda^2$. Write $\mathcal O=SO(n)\cdot W^*$ and define the **balanced-orbit threshold**
$$\kappa_n^{\mathrm{Hess}}\;:=\;\sup\left\{\frac{\tfrac12\,\mathrm{Hess}(P|_S)_{W^*}[H,H]}{\lVert D(\mathrm{Alt}\,W_-)_{W^*}[H]\rVert^2}\;:\;H\in T_{W^*}S,\ D(\mathrm{Alt}\,W_-)_{W^*}[H]\neq0\right\},$$
the second-order rate at which $P-\theta_0$ can be bought against $\delta^2$ on approach to $\mathcal O$.

**Statement.**
$$\boxed{\ \kappa_n^{\mathrm{Hess}}\;=\;\frac{27}{2}\,\theta_0(n)\left(\frac{2}{n-2}-\frac12\right)\ }$$
**the supremum being over directions with $D(\mathrm{Alt}\,W_-)_{W^*}[H]\neq0$**, and attained on [Lemma L26](nondegeneracy.md)'s direction inside **every** $(2,2)$-split four-subset, the maximiser set is the span of those $\binom m2^2$ directions, not one of them, and
$$\kappa_n^{\mathrm{Hess}}\;\le\;\kappa_n=9\big(\sqrt{3/2}-\theta_0(n)\big)\qquad\text{for every }n\ge12,$$
strictly, by a factor of $49.3$ at $n=12$ **decreasing monotonically** to the infimum $5.598$ as $n\to\infty$. **So [Conjecture L16](problem.md) is not refuted at its own conjectured maximiser, and the balanced orbit is not where its constant is decided.**

**Step 1, the spectral data of $W^*$, and $\delta$ vanishes to second order at it.** With unit factors, $R=\mathrm{id}$ on $\Lambda^2V_1\oplus\Lambda^2V_2$ and $0$ on the mixed planes $V_1\wedge V_2$, and the metric is Einstein, so $W^*$ is a multiple of $R-\tfrac{m-1}{2m-1}\mathrm{id}$: **two eigenvalues**, positive $\propto\tfrac m{2m-1}$ on $\Lambda^2V_1\oplus\Lambda^2V_2$ with multiplicity $m(m-1)$ and negative $\propto-\tfrac{m-1}{2m-1}$ on the $m^2$ mixed planes, the trace $m(m-1)\cdot m-m^2(m-1)=0$ confirming tracelessness. Hence a spectral gap at $0$ of relative size $\tfrac{m-1}{2m-1}$, so $W\mapsto W_-$ is real-analytic near $W^*$. And $W_-^*$ is a negative multiple of the projector onto the mixed planes, $=\mathrm{id}-P_{\Lambda^2V_1}-P_{\Lambda^2V_2}$, where $\mathrm{id}$ is the round-sphere operator and $P_{\Lambda^2V_i}$ is the curvature operator of $S^m\times\mathbb R^m$; each satisfies the first Bianchi identity, so $\mathrm{Alt}(W^*_-)=0$. Therefore along any curve $W(t)=W^*+tH+O(t^2)$ in $S$,
$$\delta^2(W(t))=t^2\big\lVert D(\mathrm{Alt}\,W_-)[H]\big\rVert^2+O(t^3),\qquad P(W(t))-\theta_0=\tfrac{t^2}2\mathrm{Hess}(P|_S)[H,H]+O(t^3),$$
the first-order term of $P$ vanishing by criticality, which is what makes the ratio a second-order object.

**Step 2, $\mathrm{Alt}\circ DW_-$ is supported on the $(2,2)$-split four-set blocks alone.** The coordinate $2$-planes are eigenvectors of $W^*$, so Daleckii–Krein gives $DW_-[H]$ **entrywise** in that basis: $DW_-[H]_{ab}=f^{[1]}(\lambda_a,\lambda_b)H_{ab}$ for $f(t)=t\cdot\mathbf 1_{t<0}$, i.e. coefficient $1$ on $(-,-)$ entries, $0$ on $(+,+)$ entries, and $c=\lambda_-/(\lambda_--\lambda_+)=\tfrac{m-1}{2m-1}$ on mixed ones. Being entrywise, it preserves each block of [Lemma L120](#lemma-l120-l_m-is-block-diagonal-over-the-sign-characters-at-every-diagonal-critical-point)'s decomposition. $\mathrm{Alt}$ is the projection onto the $\Lambda^4$ part, which is carried only by entries on four **distinct** indices, so diagonal and pair blocks contribute nothing. Of the four-set blocks, in the signed coordinates $(x,y,z)=(H_{ij,kl},H_{ik,jl},H_{il,jk})$ of [Lemma L121](#lemma-l121-the-4-set-block-its-spectrum-and-one-necessary-condition-per-4-subset), where first Bianchi reads $x+y+z=0$ and $\mathrm{Alt}$ is the orthogonal projection onto $\mathbf 1$:

| split of $\{i,j,k,l\}$ across the two factors | plane types of the three pairings | $DW_-[H]$ | $\mathrm{Alt}\,DW_-[H]$ |
|---|---|---|---|
| $(4,0)$ | all six planes $+$ | $0$ | $0$ |
| $(3,1)$ | each pairing couples a $+$ plane with a mixed one | $cH$ | $c\,\mathrm{Alt}(H)=0$ |
| $(2,2)$, $i,j\in V_1$, $k,l\in V_2$ | $x$ couples $\Lambda^2V_1$ with $\Lambda^2V_2$ (both $+$); $y,z$ couple two mixed planes | $(0,y,z)$ | $\tfrac{y+z}3\mathbf 1=-\tfrac x3\mathbf 1$ |

**So the denominator sees only $x$, and only on $(2,2)$-splits**, where $\lVert\mathrm{Alt}\,DW_-[H]\rVert^2=\gamma\cdot 3\cdot\tfrac{x^2}9=\tfrac\gamma3x^2$ with $\gamma$ the common weight relating the signed coordinates to the Frobenius norm, $\lVert H\rVert^2=\gamma(x^2+y^2+z^2)$. *The constant $c$ drops out entirely: the entry it multiplies is the one $\mathrm{Alt}$ annihilates.*

**Step 3, the numerator, block by block.** [Lemma L24](#lemma-l24-second-order-optimality-as-an-eigenvalue-bound-the-canonical-statement) gives $\mathrm{Hess}(P|_S)_{W^*}=6\theta_0(L_e-\tfrac12\mathrm{Id})$ on $T_{W^*}S=(W^*)^\perp$, and [Lemma L120](#lemma-l120-l_m-is-block-diagonal-over-the-sign-characters-at-every-diagonal-critical-point) makes $L_e$ block-diagonal, so numerator and denominator both split over blocks with no cross terms. At $S^m\times S^m$ the block spectra are [Lemma L127](#lemma-l127-the-block-level-refinement-of-lemma-l27-which-block-each-tfrac12-comes-from)'s:

- **four-set, $(2,2)$-split:** $L_e$ has $\pm\mu$ on $\mathbf 1^\perp$ with $\mu=\mu_{m,m}=\frac{n-2}{2(m-1)^2}=\frac2{n-2}$ ([Lemma L26](nondegeneracy.md)), the eigenvector at $+\mu$ being $(2,-1,-1)$. L26's $X=\phi_\pi\odot\phi_\rho$, whose recorded coefficients $\tfrac23,\tfrac13,-\tfrac13$ become the signed triple $(\tfrac23,-\tfrac13,-\tfrac13)$ because first Bianchi reads $T_{1234}-T_{1324}+T_{1423}=0$, so [Lemma L121](#lemma-l121-the-4-set-block-its-spectrum-and-one-necessary-condition-per-4-subset)'s signed coordinates negate the middle pairing; $\gamma=2$ then reproduces $\lVert X\rVert^2=\tfrac43$, and [r420](../reviews/REVIEW_r420.md) pinned the same $\gamma$ a second way by computing $\delta^2(W_{\mathbb{CP}^2})=\tfrac23c^2=\tfrac19$ at $c=1/\sqrt6$ by hand, and at $-\mu$ being $(0,1,-1)$, which has $x=0$;
- **four-set, $(3,1)$ and $(4,0)$:** $L_e=0$, since $A=B=C$ there makes L121's $\Sigma$ vanish;
- **pair blocks:** cross-factor spectrum exactly $\{\tfrac12\ \text{simple},0^{n-4}\}$ with the $\tfrac12$ *on the orbit*, within-factor top $\tfrac{m-2}{2(m-1)}<\tfrac12$;
- **$\mathcal D_n\cap(W^*)^\perp$:** covered by the same source as the rest, below.

**Every block has $L_e<\tfrac12$ strictly off the orbit, and this is unconditional.** [Lemma L27](nondegeneracy.md) gives $\lambda_{\max}(L_e|_{(W^*)^\perp})=\tfrac12$ **attained exactly on the orbit tangent** at every $S^p\times S^q$ with $p,q\ge3$ and $(p,q)\neq(3,3)$, which covers every $n=2m\ge12$, by the multiplicity-free $O(p)\times O(q)$ decomposition on which $L_e$ is a scalar per summand, with $\tfrac12-\mu=\frac{(p-2)(q-2)-1}{2(p-1)(q-1)}>0$; its two former grants are discharged there by proofs **uniform in $p,q,n$**. *This replaces the granted diagonal case, which an earlier version of this lemma used for the $\mathcal D_n$ block and which gives only $\preceq0$ rather than the strictness the sup needs, and it replaces a citation of [Corollary L128.1](#lemma-l128-the-branching-clause-block-checkable-nondegeneracy-at-a-pure-critical-point-and-the-isolation-it-gives), which delivers "$\tfrac12$ is not an eigenvalue" rather than "spectrum $\le\tfrac12$", the very distinction [Lemma L128](#lemma-l128-the-branching-clause-block-checkable-nondegeneracy-at-a-pure-critical-point-and-the-isolation-it-gives)'s own box installs, with $\mu_{2,4}=\tfrac23$ as its witness.* So $\mathrm{Hess}(P|_S)[H,H]\le0$ for every $H$ normal to the orbit, and it is $<0$ on every block except the orbit's own.

**Step 4, the sup, and it is one direction.** Decompose $H=\sum_bH_b$ over blocks. The denominator is $\sum_{b\ (2,2)}\tfrac\gamma3x_b^2$ and the numerator is $\sum_b\mathrm{num}_b$ with every $\mathrm{num}_b\le0$; a ratio of sums with nonpositive numerators and nonnegative denominators is at most the largest per-block ratio, so the sup is attained inside a single $(2,2)$-split block. There, writing $H=\alpha(2,-1,-1)+\beta(0,1,-1)$, the whole of $\mathbf 1^\perp$, 
$$\frac{\tfrac12\mathrm{Hess}[H,H]}{\lVert\mathrm{Alt}\,DW_-[H]\rVert^2}=\frac{3\theta_0\gamma\big[(\mu-\tfrac12)6\alpha^2-(\mu+\tfrac12)2\beta^2\big]}{\tfrac\gamma3\,4\alpha^2},$$
maximised at $\beta=0$, where it equals $\frac{18\theta_0(\mu-\tfrac12)}{4/3}=\frac{27}2\theta_0\big(\mu-\tfrac12\big)$. **The weight $\gamma$ cancels**, so no normalisation convention enters the answer. Substituting $\mu=\tfrac2{n-2}$ gives the boxed formula.

**Step 5, the comparison with $\kappa_n$, in one line.** $\kappa_n^{\mathrm{Hess}}\le\kappa_n$ reads $\tfrac32\theta_0\big(\tfrac2{n-2}-\tfrac12\big)\le\sqrt{3/2}-\theta_0$, i.e.
$$\theta_0(n)\left(\frac3{n-2}+\frac14\right)\;\le\;\sqrt{3/2}.$$
The bracket decreases in $n$, so at $n\ge12$ it is at most $\tfrac3{10}+\tfrac14=0.55$; and $\theta_0(n)=\sqrt{2(n-1)(n-2)}/n<\sqrt2$ for every $n$, since $(n-1)(n-2)<n^2$. Hence the left side is below $0.55\sqrt2=0.778<1.2247=\sqrt{3/2}$. $\square$

**The numbers, as a check rather than as evidence.** $n=12$: $\mu=\tfrac15$, $\theta_0=\sqrt{220}/12=1.23603$, $\kappa^{\mathrm{Hess}}_{12}=-5.006$ against $\kappa_{12}=9(1.224745-1.236033)=-0.10159$, a factor of $49$. As $n\to\infty$: $\mu\to0$, $\theta_0\to\sqrt2$, $\kappa^{\mathrm{Hess}}\to-\tfrac{27}{4}\sqrt2=-9.546$ against $\kappa_\infty=9(\sqrt{3/2}-\sqrt2)=-1.705$, a factor of $5.6$, which is the ratio's minimum over $n\ge12$.

**An independent consistency check of the formula.** At $n=6$ it returns $\mu=\tfrac12$ and $\kappa^{\mathrm{Hess}}=0$, and $n=6$ is exactly where the record reports $\dim E(\tfrac12)=18$ at $S^3\times S^3$ with nine of those eigenvectors in four-set blocks ([Lemma L126](#lemma-l126-the-four-set-tfrac12-threshold-met-in-the-two-factor-family-only-at-s3times-s3), [Lemma L127](#lemma-l127-the-block-level-refinement-of-lemma-l27-which-block-each-tfrac12-comes-from)), the threshold vanishes precisely where the block becomes degenerate, which no step of the derivation was arranged to produce.

**What this settles and what it leaves.** *Settles*: the (α) scenario of [Conjecture L16](problem.md)'s stability formulation, in closed form and for every $n\ge12$ at once. *Leaves*: the third-order remainder, without which $\kappa^{\mathrm{Hess}}_n\le\kappa_n$ does not yet give a **radius** $r_n$ on which L16 holds, $P$ is cubic so $D^3P$ is constant, and $\delta^2$'s third derivative is controlled through the spectral gap, so the remainder is a bounded computation. **What it must be beaten against is definiteness, which [r420](../reviews/REVIEW_r420.md) supplied**: the quadratic form $\tfrac12\mathrm{Hess}(P|_S)-\kappa_n\lVert D(\mathrm{Alt}\,W_-)[\cdot]\rVert^2$ vanishes **exactly** on $T_{W^*}\mathcal O$ and is therefore negative definite on its complement, so it dominates $-c\lVert H\rVert^2$ there. *Does not touch*: the (β) scenario at the $\mathbb{CP}^2$-type competitor, where $W$ has a kernel and $\delta^2$ has a kink ([Lemma L17](route-a.md)), nor the (γ) interior scenario, which is the conjecture. **The lemma depends on the granted diagonal case nowhere**, [Lemma L27](nondegeneracy.md) covering every block; and the reduction to the orbit's normal space is licensed by the $O(n)$-invariance of $P$ and of $\delta^2$, which makes both the numerator and the denominator constant along the orbit.

#### Lemma L141, the defect's derivative is a $\tfrac12$-contraction at every critical point, and the balanced orbit's penalised form is definite with an explicit constant

*Derived 2026-09-03 as [I2](../plan.md)(iii) on route [R17](../routes.md). **Gate 2 owed.** Part (a) is [Lemma L140](route-a.md)'s recentring stated once, for every base point rather than only at the competitor.*

**Statement.**

> **(a) A $\tfrac12$-contraction, everywhere, degenerate spectrum included.** Let $V\in\mathcal W_n$ be **any** tensor and $H\in\mathcal W_n$, the derivative being one-sided where $V$ has a kernel. Then
> $$\big\lVert D(\mathrm{Alt}\,W_-)_V[H]\big\rVert\;\le\;\tfrac12\lVert H\rVert .$$
> *Proof*: $\mathrm{Alt}(H)=0$ since $H$ satisfies the first Bianchi identity, so $\mathrm{Alt}(DW_-[H])=\mathrm{Alt}(DW_-[H]+\mu H)$ for every $\mu$; the Daleckii–Krein multiplier of $f(x)=\tfrac12(\lvert x\rvert-x)$ has every entry in $[-1,0]$, being a divided difference of a function with slopes $0$ and $-1$; centring at $\mu=\tfrac12$ makes every entry of $DW_-[H]+\tfrac12H$ at most half the corresponding entry of $H$; and $\mathrm{Alt}$ is an orthogonal projection. **On a kernel block, where the multiplier does not exist, the exact value is $D_-$ and $D_-+\tfrac12D=\tfrac12\lvert D\rvert$, of norm $\tfrac12\lVert D\rVert$**, so the degenerate case is covered by the same constant rather than excluded, which is what lets the statement hold at the competitor. *An earlier version hypothesised differentiable spectral parts and then claimed the competitor, where they are not; [r430](../reviews/REVIEW_r430.md) caught the mismatch.* $\square$ **The constant is the half-length of the multiplier's range and nothing else, so it is the same in either sign convention.**
>
> **(b) Definiteness at the balanced orbit, with the sharp constant.** At $W^*$ with $n=2m\ge12$, write $Q(H)=\tfrac12\mathrm{Hess}(P|_S)_{W^*}[H,H]-\kappa_n\lVert D(\mathrm{Alt}\,W_-)[H]\rVert^2$ on $T_{W^*}S\cap T_{W^*}\mathcal O^\perp$. Then
> $$Q(H)\;\le\;-\,c_n\lVert H\rVert^2,\qquad c_n\;=\;\frac{3\,\theta_0(n)}{n-2},$$
> **and this is attained, so the constant is unimprovable.**

**Proof of (b).** $Q$ is additive over [Lemma L120](#lemma-l120-l_m-is-block-diagonal-over-the-sign-characters-at-every-diagonal-critical-point)'s blocks, both of its terms being quadratic forms that the block decomposition diagonalises. **The defect response vanishes on every block but the $(2,2)$-split four-sets**, the argument is [Lemma L133](#lemma-l133-the-balanced-orbit-stability-threshold-in-closed-form-and-it-clears-kappa_n-for-every-nge12)'s support table, so on all other blocks $Q=\tfrac12\mathrm{Hess}$, which [Lemma L27](nondegeneracy.md) bounds by $3\theta_0(\lambda_m-\tfrac12)=-\tfrac{3\theta_0}{n-2}$ per unit norm off the orbit, **attained on the within-factor pair block**. **On the $(2,2)$ four-sets the comparison is settled by one monotonicity computation, and the sentence that stood here before is struck.** *That sentence multiplied a **negative** bound by $\sigma$ and then bounded $\sigma$ **above**, the wrong direction, fatal independently of the infimum-ratio substitution it also made, and unsalvageable ([r441](../reviews/REVIEW_r441.md), which found two defects where [r439](../reviews/REVIEW_r439.md) found one).* Writing $s$ for the fraction of a direction's weight carried by the defect-active part of the block,
$$\frac{Q}{\lVert H\rVert^2}=3\theta_0\big(2\mu s-\mu-\tfrac12\big)+\tfrac29\lvert\kappa_n\rvert s,$$
**increasing in $s$**, so its maximum sits at $s=1$, that is, at $\sigma=\tfrac29$, [Lemma L133](#lemma-l133-the-balanced-orbit-stability-threshold-in-closed-form-and-it-clears-kappa_n-for-every-nge12)'s own step-2 bound. *Without this sentence the comparison is not established, and an earlier version substituted the infimum ratio $5.598$ where the $n$ in hand supplies $49.27$ at $n=12$.* **So the pair block binds, and the minimum is exactly $-\tfrac{3\theta_0}{n-2}$.** $\square$

**The domain is $T_{W^*}S\cap T_{W^*}\mathcal O^\perp$ and the intersection is load-bearing.** *Orthogonality to the orbit inside $\mathcal W_n$ is not enough*: $H=W^*$ itself is orthogonal to $T_{W^*}\mathcal O$ and gives $Q(W^*)=+3\theta_0>0$, since $L_e$ has eigenvalue $1$ there. **The radial direction must be excluded by hand** ([r431](../reviews/REVIEW_r431.md)).

**Why a crossing argument does not work here.** Combining the two bounds by taking their minimum over $\sigma$ locates the worst case at their crossing, giving $2.464\,\theta_0/(n-2)$. *That reasoning was defective, the first bound increases in $\sigma$ rather than decreasing, so the stated reason did not support the stated conclusion, and it was also unnecessary*: the two bounds live on **different blocks**, not on one direction, so there is nothing to trade off. The block argument gives the larger constant and needs neither part (a) nor $\kappa_n^{\mathrm{Hess}}$ on the binding block.

**The $1/n$ decay is intrinsic.** $c_n=3\theta_0/(n-2)$ is $3\theta_0$ times the balanced product's own **$L_e$-eigenvalue gap** $\tfrac12-\lambda_m=\tfrac1{n-2}$, *the Hessian is $6\theta_0(L_e-\tfrac12)$ and $Q$ carries half of it; an earlier version said $6\theta_0$ ([r442](../reviews/REVIEW_r442.md))*, which no argument can improve. *This is a gap in the $L_e$ spectrum and must not be confused with the $\Lambda^2$ spectral gap $g_n$ that the resolvent needs, nor with the relative gap $\tfrac{m-1}{2m-1}$, [r439](../reviews/REVIEW_r439.md) found the last of those three misused as "the spectral gap" elsewhere in the record, overstating an analyticity radius by a factor $n$.* *So the tube's radius question is entirely about the third-order constant, not about this one.*

**What (a) is for, now that (b) does not use it.** It is the general form of the estimate that closed [I3](../plan.md): *the defect's first-order response is a $\tfrac12$-contraction of the direction, for the single reason that $\mathrm{Alt}$ annihilates Weyl tensors and a divided-difference multiplier of $x_-$ has range of length one.* **The constant is the same at every base point**, but the argument is not: where $V$ is invertible these three lines suffice, and where it is degenerate [Lemma L140](route-a.md)'s extra identity on the kernel block is needed to reach it. *Its $\sigma\le\tfrac14$ played no role in (b) and is not claimed to: [Lemma L133](#lemma-l133-the-balanced-orbit-stability-threshold-in-closed-form-and-it-clears-kappa_n-for-every-nge12)'s own support table gives the sharper $\sigma\le\tfrac29$ on the only block where $\sigma\ne0$.*

**What is still owed for the tube.** The third-order remainder $C$, after which the radius is $r_n=c_n/C$. $P$ is a cubic form, so its own remainder along a great circle is $\sin^3t\,P(H)$ **plus two further third-order tails** and needs a bound on $\max_{\lVert X\rVert=1}\lvert P(X)\rvert$; the defect's remainder is the harder half and is controlled by the resolvent through the **absolute** gap $g_n=\Theta(1/n)$ at $W^*$, *not the relative $\tfrac{m-1}{2m-1}$, which overstates the radius by a factor $n$ ([r439](../reviews/REVIEW_r439.md), [r441](../reviews/REVIEW_r441.md))*. **Note $c_n$ decays like $1/n$**, so the radius does too unless $C$ decays with it.

## Post F60, WITHDRAWN: the two pinned halves live on orthogonal blocks and never bind together

**REFUTED at Gate 2 by [r442](../reviews/REVIEW_r442.md), on the joint the brief named.** The post argued that $r_n=c_n/C$ is a ceiling because both halves are pinned, the margin attained, the remainder forced. **They are pinned on *different blocks*.** $c_n$ is attained on the within-factor **pair** block, and on every pair block $D(\mathrm{Alt}\,W_-)[H]=0$ by [Lemma L133](#lemma-l133-the-balanced-orbit-stability-threshold-in-closed-form-and-it-clears-kappa_n-for-every-nge12)'s own support table. *So along the direction attaining the margin, $A(0)=A'(0)=0$ and the entire resolvent half of $C$ is inert.* **A margin from one block divided by a remainder from another is not a pinned quotient.**

**What actually sets the exponent is a third constant the post never named**: $C_P=O(n)$, from $\sup\lvert P\rvert\le n-1$ ([Lemma L107](bounds.md)). A direction-wise estimate with $v$ the weight on the $(2,2)$ four-sets gives $r_n\ge6\min_v\big(c_{\mathrm{pair}}+(c_4-c_{\mathrm{pair}})v^2\big)/(C_P+Knv)$: at $C_P=\Theta(n)$ the minimum sits at $v=0$ and returns $\Theta(n^{-2})$, **but at $C_P=\Theta(1)$ it sits at $v^*=\Theta(n^{-1/2})$ and returns $\Theta(n^{-3/2})$.** *Since $\sup\lvert P\rvert$ is attained at a critical point and every recorded one is $O(1)$, the $n-1$ bound is likely off by a factor $n$, so the exponent is not fixed at all.*

**Two further defects, both mine.** *A factor of two*: $c_n=3\theta_0/(n-2)$ is $3\theta_0$ times the gap $\tfrac1{n-2}$, not $6\theta_0$, the Hessian is $6\theta_0(L_e-\tfrac12)$ and $Q$ carries half of it. *And an upper bound sold as a lower one*: [r441](../reviews/REVIEW_r441.md) wrote $r_n=\Theta(n^{-2})$ **absent an unproven cancellation** in $\langle A'',A'\rangle$, and the post quoted it with the condition dropped, *a cancellation that is not idle, $\mathrm{Alt}$ having already killed the $\Theta(1)$ Neumann term and the Daleckii–Krein coefficient at orders $0$ and $1$ of the same chain.*

**[r443](../reviews/REVIEW_r443.md), independently, reaches the same verdict and names the structure.** The two extrema sit on **orthogonal eigenspaces of $L_e$**: [Lemma L27](nondegeneracy.md) puts the off-orbit margin **minimum** on the two $S^2_0(\mathbb R^m)$ summands, *$\tfrac12-\lambda_m=\tfrac1{2(m-1)}$ against $\tfrac12-\mu=\tfrac{m-3}{2(m-1)}$, and it is the minimum that a convex combination reads*, and [Lemma L133](#lemma-l133-the-balanced-orbit-stability-threshold-in-closed-form-and-it-clears-kappa_n-for-every-nge12)'s support table puts the defect response on the $+\mu$ eigendirection of the $(2,2)$ blocks, distinct eigenvalues of a symmetric operator, hence orthogonal. **And the shape of the error is simpler than either instance: dividing a $\min$ by a $\max$ is a *lower* bound on the radius, and a fence needs the opposite direction.**

**It also improves the radius, with no new instrument**: per-direction bookkeeping using the record's own Cauchy estimate $\lVert A^{(k)}\rVert=O(n^{k-1})$ and the block-additivity of $Q$ gives $r_n=\Omega(n^{-3/2})$, *better by $\sqrt n$, and still inside the analyticity ball $g_n=\Theta(1/n)$, which $\Theta(n^{-1})$ would not be.* **And $f^{[3]}$ enters only $A'''$, which $\mathrm{Alt}(W^*_-)=0$ annihilates**, so the *second* divided difference governs and the post's citation of the third was inert.

**What survives as fact.** The divided differences, recomputed cold: $f^{[2]}=-m^2\sqrt{m-1}/(2m-1)^{3/2}\sim-n/(4\sqrt2)$ and $f^{[3]}=-m^3(m-1)/(2m-1)^2\sim-n^2/16$. And $c_n$'s attainment, re-derived from [Lemma L27](nondegeneracy.md). **What does not survive is the ceiling**, and a successor is free to seek a better exponent, the two moves that would change it are the same Taylor argument read more carefully.

**One counter-route recorded so it is not retried**: the gap-free $\tfrac12$-Lipschitz bound $\delta^2\le t^2/4$ loses at second order by a factor $n$, $\lvert\kappa_n\rvert=\Theta(1)$ against a $\Theta(1/n)$ margin.

## Post F50, the four-set concentration route: the lemma is false, and both projections fail in opposite ways

*[r389](../reviews/REVIEW_r389.md) and [r390](../reviews/REVIEW_r390.md), identical briefs, agreeing and each deriving the two-factor family's closed form cold. **[Lemma L128](second-order.md)(ii) is untouched**, at every witness below $\Sigma=0$ exactly, so what falls is the route, not the clause.*

**The lemma is false.** "$m_4(S)\ge\tfrac12$ at some four-subset forces $\theta<\sqrt{3/2}$" fails at $S^5\times S^{43}$, $n=48$: in band at $\theta=1.263631$, with a four-subset carrying $m_4=0.501203$. At $S^5\times S^{1000}$ a single four-subset holds $59.5\%$ of the mass, and the sufficiency $3m_4<\theta^2$ fails outright from $S^5\times S^{66}$. **No constant repairs an $m_4$-only criterion**, since the band allows $\theta^2/3<2/3$ while $m_4\to3/5$.

**And $p=5$ is the unique in-band failure, which is structural.** The band's floor is $\min(p,q)\ge5$, the reduction wants $p\ge6$, and $m_4\ge\tfrac12$ wants $p\le5$: the two conditions meet at exactly one value of $p$ and the band sits on it. *Testing balanced products never sees it.*

**Both projections fail, in opposite ways.** Onto $\mathcal D_n$: cheap, but the result has support on all of $[n]$, so only $\Theta_n\to\sqrt2$ applies, the bound one already had. Onto $\mathcal D_n\cap\{\text{four-supported}\}$: keeps $\Theta_4=\sqrt{12}/4$, but that space is the $2$-plane $\{x=x',y=y',z=z',x+y+z=0\}$ and **the extremal concentrator is orthogonal to it**, so the projection is identically zero.

**And the repaired plan dies on an equality.** The well-posedness objection, $\theta$ is defined at critical points while $M_S$ is not critical, is repaired in one line by $\theta(M):=\sqrt2\,P(M)/\lVert M\rVert^3$, the functional [Lemma L57](problem.md) actually bounds. Then the maximal four-supported concentrator sits at **exactly $\sqrt{3/2}$**, the band constant: dropping zero-row-sum raises the four-support supremum by precisely that. *No sharpening recovers a strict inequality from an equality.*

**What survives, and it is worth more than the route.** $\Sigma=\tfrac32(A^2+B^2+C^2)-\tfrac12\sigma^2$ is an **identity**, with $\sigma=A+B+C=\sum_{a<b\in S}M_{ab}$, so $\Sigma\le3m_4-\tfrac12\sigma^2$, **equality on the $(4,0)$ sets and the *balanced* $(2,2)$ splits**, *corrected at [r400](../reviews/REVIEW_r400.md), which refutes the wider claim at $S^5\times S^7$ ($0.2776$ against $0.2619$), at $S^5\times S^{43}$ ($0.2423$ against $0.1197$) and at every $(3,1)$ set*, clearing $S^5\times S^{200}$ with the full budget and reproving [Lemma L126](second-order.md) in three lines. **A successor uses the identity, not the mass alone.** *And it is unimprovable in its own coordinates: [post F52](INDEX-traps.md) records the $(\rho,m_4)$ recoordinatisation and why the extra term is inert.*

## Post F51, every rearrangement of the pair-clause data is [post F47](INDEX-traps.md)'s eigen-equation, and inert

*Recorded after eight successive attempts on [I18](../plan.md)'s clauses, the last four refuted at Gate 2 by [r395](../reviews/REVIEW_r395.md)–[r398](../reviews/REVIEW_r398.md). This generalises [post F49](INDEX-traps.md) from conditions on a hypothetical eigenvector to reformulations of the data itself.*

**The fact.** The pair block's whole content is $\widetilde N=M|_I+\mathop{\mathrm{diag}}(p)$ with $p_a=M_{aj}+M_{ak}$, and criticality gives $(M\tilde o)_a=\tilde o_a(\theta-p_a)$ on $I$, [post F47](INDEX-traps.md)'s eigen-equation. **Every reformulation attempted since has been that identity rearranged**: the four-set discrepancy as orbit-vector differences, the extension of $o$ to $[n]$, the "constant residual", the approximate-eigenvector reading. Each was new as an arrangement and none added information.

**The tell, and it is cheap.** A rearrangement carries no new input, so **it cannot beat a bound already derived from the same input**. Two checks catch it before a cycle is spent:

- **Does the new form use a quantity the old one did not?** If every symbol on both sides is built from $M$'s entries and $\theta$, the answer is no.
- **Evaluate it against the incumbent at one recorded point.** The orbit form landed $7$–$10\times$ looser than [post F50](INDEX-traps.md)'s mass identity; the spectral bound landed $1.5\times$ weaker than [Lemma L68](diagonal-half.md), which is an *identity* for the same quantity. *Both comparisons take one line and neither was run.*

**What would not be inert.** A statement that consumes something the eigen-equation does not: the **entry-spread** of $o$ rather than its norm (L68 prices the norm; $\Sigma$ needs the spread, and every conversion spends the factor the incumbent keeps); the **coupling to the complement** $X$ in $\Sigma=3m_4+\sigma^2-3\theta\sigma+3X$; or the band **as structure** rather than as the number $\theta^2\ge\tfrac32$, for which [I20](../plan.md) was the candidate and is closed.

**The count, so a successor knows the shape of the ground.** Six routes on the pair clause (perturbation, variational ascent, trace–Frobenius, rank-one split, joint estimate, trace bound on $\mathbf1^\perp$), then the four-set mass route, then the orbit reformulation, **eight, every one refuted by an argument the attempt could have run on itself.**

## Post F52, the four-set's complement coupling is determined by $\theta$ and four row sums, hence inert

*[r399](../reviews/REVIEW_r399.md) and [r400](../reviews/REVIEW_r400.md), agreeing. It closes one of [post F51](INDEX-traps.md)'s three named live inputs, the one that looked most promising, since the complement is what every earlier attempt discarded.*

**The fact.** For a four-subset $S$, the coupling $X=\sum_{a<b\in S}\sum_{c\notin S}M_{ac}M_{cb}$ satisfies, at every pure critical point,
$$X=\theta\sigma-\tfrac12\sum_{a\in S}\rho_a^2,\qquad \rho_a=\sum_{b\in S}M_{ab},\quad\sigma=\sum_{a<b\in S}M_{ab}.$$
*Proof: $t=(M\mathbf1_S)|_{S^c}$, so $\sum_ct_c^2=\lVert M\mathbf1_S\rVert^2-\sum_a\rho_a^2$; [Lemma L129](diagonal-half.md)'s $M^2=\theta M+L(w)$ gives $\lVert M\mathbf1_S\rVert^2=2\theta\sigma+E$ with $E$ the cross mass; and $E$ cancels against $\sum_cw_c$ in $X=\tfrac12\sum_c(t_c^2-w_c)$.*

**So the complement enters $\Sigma$ through nothing but $\theta$ and the four within-$S$ row sums**, every one of which is already a function of the six entries $\Sigma$ is built from. **A route that reaches outside $S$ through $X$ therefore imports no information**, which is the same verdict [post F51](INDEX-traps.md) reached for rearrangements inside $S$, now extended to the one direction that looked open.

**What it leaves of F51's three.** The **entry-spread** of $o$ against its norm, and the **band as structure**, are untouched; [I20](../plan.md) was the candidate for the second and is closed. *A successor proposing to use the complement must say what it uses beyond $X$, the flow vector $t$ coordinatewise, say, rather than through $\sum t_c^2$, since only the sum is pinned.*

**And it corrects [post F50](INDEX-traps.md) in passing.** That post's "equality on the whole two-factor family" is false: equality holds on the **balanced** family, and fails at every unbalanced $(2,2)$ set, $0.2776$ against $0.2619$ at $S^5\times S^7$, $0.2423$ against $0.1197$ at $S^5\times S^{43}$, and at every $(3,1)$ set.

## Post F53, the spectral gap does NOT generalise to $\Lambda^2$: every unit Weyl operator in the band has an eigenvalue in $(0,\theta)$

*[r405](../reviews/REVIEW_r405.md) and [r406](../reviews/REVIEW_r406.md), agreeing, each measuring at named critical points. It closes the $\#$-positivity route to [I1](../plan.md) the same day it was opened.*

**The fact, in one line.** $\mathop{\mathrm{tr}}W=0$ for every Weyl operator on $\Lambda^2$, and $\lVert W\rVert=1$ gives $\sum_\alpha\lambda_\alpha^2=1$. A traceless nonzero operator has a positive eigenvalue, and $\lambda_{\max}^2\le1$ gives $\lambda_{\max}<1<\theta$ throughout the band. **So $\mathop{\mathrm{spec}}(W)\cap(0,\theta)\ne\varnothing$ always, criticality is not even needed.** *Screened: $30$ of $66$ eigenvalues of $S^6\times S^6$ lie inside.*

**What it fences.** [Lemma L129](diagonal-half.md)'s gap is a statement about the $n\times n$ **sectional matrix** $K$ and **has no $\Lambda^2$ analogue**. Any route that hopes to bound $\theta$ off the diagonal locus by excluding eigenvalues from $(0,\theta)$ is closed before it starts. **And the $\#$-positivity reading closes with it**: criticality gives $\langle v,W^\#v\rangle=\lambda(\theta-\lambda)$ for every eigenvector, *correct, and on record three times, with [route R14](../routes.md) closed on it*, so $W^\#$ is positive on every positive eigendirection, of which there is always at least one. **There is nothing to prove and no sign to find.**

**And the deeper identification, which is what actually went wrong** ([r406](../reviews/REVIEW_r406.md)): the section identified $\mathop{\mathrm{spec}}_{\Lambda^2}(W)$ with $\mathop{\mathrm{spec}}(K)$ for the sectional matrix. **They are different objects**, on the diagonal locus the $\Lambda^2$ spectrum is the multiset of *entries* of $K$, while [Lemma L129](diagonal-half.md) constrains its *eigenvalues*. *Witnesses: padded $W_{\mathbb{CP}^2}$ has $\lambda_{\max}=0.8165$ against $\theta=1.2247$, and $S^6\times S^6$ has $0.135$ against $1.236$.*

**The trap that produced it, and it is the record's own.** $\sum\lambda_\alpha^2=2$ was taken from [Lemma L129](diagonal-half.md)'s $\mathop{\mathrm{tr}}K^2\le2$ for the **sectional** matrix and applied to the $\Lambda^2$ operator, then multiplied against $\mathop{\mathrm{tr}}W^\#=-1$, which is quoted in the other convention. [conventions.md](conventions.md) names this exact trap, the operator-level $\tfrac12$ "inflates every $\theta$ by $\sqrt2$, and has cost a cycle", **and it has now cost a second.** *The letter is the live half of it: $M$ is the $n\times n$ vertex matrix in [diagonal-half.md](diagonal-half.md) and $W$ is the $\Lambda^2$ operator in [bispectral.md](bispectral.md), with $\lVert K\rVert_F^2=2\lVert W\rVert^2$; [conventions.md](conventions.md) flags the letter $B$ and owes a row for $M$.* *The tell was available for free: $\mathop{\mathrm{tr}}Q(W)=\theta\mathop{\mathrm{tr}}W$ reads $1+(-1)=0$, so the relation it produces is vacuous rather than informative.*

## Post F54, re-bounding $H$'s off-diagonal blocks by trace data cannot close $J_5\ge0$

*[r409](../reviews/REVIEW_r409.md), with [r410](../reviews/REVIEW_r410.md) agreeing that the proposal it fences is relabelling rather than progress.*

**The fact.** Any bound on $\lVert H_{bc}\rVert_F$ built from the diagonal blocks' **trace data** is blind to the case $H$ scalar, where $H_{bc}=0$ and the cross term vanishes exactly, and carries an unavoidable factor $\sqrt{d_bd_c}=\Theta(n^2)$. *A necessary condition for such a bound to close the grant is $d_bd_c\le1$, impossible for $n\ge4$.*

**Why it looks promising and is not.** $C=W_+\#W_-$ and $H=\mathrm{Ric}^*(\mathrm{Ric}(|W|))$ are both PSD, so the block bound $\lVert A_{bc}\rVert_F\le\sqrt{\mathop{\mathrm{tr}}A_{bb}\mathop{\mathrm{tr}}A_{cc}}$ applies to each, **but the record's Theorem C is already the *joint* Cauchy–Schwarz on $U_b=C^{1/2}P_bH^{1/2}$, with $m_b=\mathop{\mathrm{tr}}(C_{bb}H_{bb})$ carrying $H$ inside.** Splitting the pairing is a strict loss, and it is **largest exactly in the favourable regime**. *[Theorem D](../ATTEMPTS.md)'s gap-commutator certificate strictly dominates any such bound, since it vanishes on the commuting class.*

**And the condition number is not the missing factor, the record already bounds it from below, unboundedly.** [ATTEMPTS.md](../ATTEMPTS.md)'s *Ricci spread $K$* section studies the same matrix ($\mathrm{Ric}(|W|)=2\mathrm{Ric}(W_-)$), giving $\kappa\ge K$ with $K_{\max}=n/4$ and exactly $\kappa=q/p$ at $S^p\times S^q$. $\kappa(\mathrm{Ric}(|W|))=+\infty$ at Fox and at the plateau, where $h$ has zero padding blocks and $J_5\ge0$ is sharp; isotropy makes $h$ block-scalar rather than scalar, so $\kappa>1$ already at unbalanced sphere products.

**And the blind spot itself is proven ground.** $H=\mathrm{Ric}^*(h)$ is diagonal in the eigenframe of $h=\mathrm{Ric}(\lvert W\rvert)$ with eigenvalues $\eta_i+\eta_j$ on $e_i\wedge e_j$, the Kulkarni–Nomizu expansion of $h\owedge\mathrm{id}$ has no other surviving term for $i<j$, $k<l$, so $h=\lambda\,\mathrm{id}$ makes $H=2\lambda\,\mathrm{Id}$ and $J_5=\lambda\mathop{\mathrm{scal}}(X_{\mathrm{inv}})\ge0$ by Result 5(a); [Lemma L154](bounds.md) carries the equality case. *[r697](../reviews/REVIEW_r697.md), [r698](../reviews/REVIEW_r698.md); the diagonality is checked against the numerically built adjoint to $5\times10^{-15}$.* **A corollary [r698](../reviews/REVIEW_r698.md) draws**: $\lambda_{\min}(H)=\eta_{(1)}+\eta_{(2)}$, the two smallest eigenvalues of $h$, the shift constant the surviving repair below needs, in closed form.

**The repair that does survive** ([r410](../reviews/REVIEW_r410.md)): shift by $\lambda_{\min}(H)$, free since $H-\lambda\mathrm{Id}\succeq0$ keeps the cross blocks, giving a bound that **vanishes with $H_{\mathrm{cross}}$** and the criterion $\kappa\le1+\mathop{\mathrm{tr}}X_{\mathrm{inv}}/(4D)$, scaling as $\kappa-1=O(n^{-2})$, a sharpening of the commuting class rather than a route past it.

**What to do instead, with the caveat that this is Result 5(b) restated.** Drop the block/cross split: $J_5=4\langle C,H\rangle-2\langle\theta W_-+W_-^2,H\rangle$ with no cross term, so **the grant is the frame-free $2\langle W_+\#W_-,H\rangle\ge\langle\theta W_-+W_-^2,H\rangle$** between pairings of PSD operators. *This is [ATTEMPTS.md](../ATTEMPTS.md)'s Result 5(b) with $X_{\mathrm{inv}}$ substituted, $\mathrm{Ric}^*$ being $\mathrm{Ric}$'s adjoint; and its operator form is the refuted $X_{\mathrm{inv}}\succeq0$. So it reframes rather than opens.* *If the block route is kept at all, the remaining slack is in summing three individually-sharp pair bounds over a $3\times3$ Gram matrix.*

## Post F55, the three-term $\#$-form of the grant has zero $\Lambda^4$ content, and the $\#$-trace is inert against $W$ and $I$

*Two reviewers, independently, on identical briefs: [r413](../reviews/REVIEW_r413.md), [r414](../reviews/REVIEW_r414.md).*

**The setting.** [Lemma L131](isotropy-cap.md) gives $\mathrm{Ric}(R)\wedge\mathrm{id}=R+R\#I-3\,\mathrm{Alt}(R)$ for every symmetric $R$. Full symmetry of Huisken's $\mathrm{tri}$ moves $\#I$ off its argument, $\langle X,R\#I\rangle=\mathop{\mathrm{tr}}(X\#R)$, which is self-adjointness of $R\mapsto R\#I$, so the grant reads as an inner product, a $\#$-trace and a defect pairing. **The three recombine to $\tfrac12 J_5$ by the identity that produced them**, $J_5$ being symmetric in its two slots; the form is the grant's own definition in other symbols.

**The grant at its native argument.** At $\lvert W\rvert=W+2W_-$ the $\langle X,W\rangle$ terms cancel identically, by $W\#I=-W$ and $\mathrm{Alt}(W)=0$:
$$\boxed{\ J_5\;=\;4\Big[\big\langle X_{\mathrm{inv}},W_-\big\rangle+\mathop{\mathrm{tr}}\big(X_{\mathrm{inv}}\#W_-\big)-3\big\langle X_{\mathrm{inv}},\mathrm{Alt}(W_-)\big\rangle\Big]\;=\;2\big\langle\mathrm{Ric}(X_{\mathrm{inv}}),g\big\rangle,\quad g=\mathrm{Ric}(W_-)=\mathrm{Ric}(W_+)\succeq0\ }$$
using $\mathrm{Ric}(W)=0$, hence $\mathrm{Ric}(\lvert W\rvert)=2g$. The defect now stands at the argument it is defined on, $\delta^2=\lVert\mathrm{Alt}(W_-)\rVert^2$; the low-rank $W_-$ replaces full-support $\lvert W\rvert$.

**What the post fences.** Two things a successor would otherwise look for.

- **No $\Lambda^4$ cancellation to hunt.** $\#I$ acts by $c_4=2$ on $\Lambda^4$, so $\mathrm{Alt}(\lvert W\rvert+\lvert W\rvert\#I)=3\,\mathrm{Alt}(\lvert W\rvert)$ and the third term cancels the counter-term exactly. **The three-term form carries no $\Lambda^4$ information at all**, which closes the search for a cancellation beside [Result 2](../results.md)'s $\tfrac32$-Gram and $\mathop{\mathrm{tr}}D=6\lVert\mathrm{Alt}\rVert^2$.
- **The $\#$-trace is strong only where it is already determined.** $\mathop{\mathrm{tr}}(X\#W)=-\langle X,W\rangle$ and $I\#I=(n-2)I$ collapse it against either Bianchi argument. $W_-$ is the sole argument leaving the Bianchi subspace, so $\mathop{\mathrm{tr}}(X_{\mathrm{inv}}\#W_-)$ is the one pairing that carries content, and the one place the calculus has no scalar to offer.

**Two corrections it carries.** $H=2(\lvert W\rvert+\lvert W\rvert\#I-3\mathrm{Alt}(\lvert W\rvert))$ has a factor $2$, so the bracket alone is $J_5/2$, inert for the sign, live in the master identity and $p=(J_4-J_5)/r^2$. And $\theta W_-+W_-^2\succeq0$ needs $\theta\ge-\lambda^+_{\min}(W_-)$, which [bounds-relaxations.md](bounds-relaxations.md) shows is not free: it catalogues critical points with $\theta\le0$.

**Splitting $J_5$ into two nonnegative pieces is inert.** $J_5=4\langle C,H\rangle-2\langle\theta W_-+W_-^2,H\rangle$ already does it by Frobenius pairing of PSD operators, with no $\#$-calculus, and is [Result 5(a)](../ATTEMPTS.md) at operator level. An unsigned difference of two nonnegative numbers is strictly weaker than the signed bound $J_5\ge J_5^{\mathrm{diag}}-\lvert\mathrm{cross}\rvert$ the block form gives.

## Post F56, the polarised scal identity returns $J_5$ at EVERY argument built from $W$'s spectral parts, because $\mathrm{Ric}$ has rank-one image there

*Corrected at Gate 2 ([r418](../reviews/REVIEW_r418.md)): the post first argued this from a substitution that proves nothing, and the true reason is stronger than the claim it was offered for.*

**The mechanism.** $\mathrm{Ric}(W)=0$ and $\mathrm{Ric}(W_+)=\mathrm{Ric}(W_-)=g$, so $\mathrm{Ric}$ restricted to $\mathrm{span}\{W_+,W_-\}$ has **rank one**. Hence for every $B=\alpha W_++\beta W_-$ the record's polarised scalar-curvature identity evaluated against $X_{\mathrm{inv}}$ returns
$$\tfrac12\big\langle\mathrm{Ric}(X_{\mathrm{inv}}),\mathrm{Ric}(B)\big\rangle=\tfrac{\alpha+\beta}{4}\,J_5 ,$$
**a multiple of $J_5$ and nothing else.** So no choice of argument inside that span extracts new information: the $\lvert W\rvert$ reading and the $W_-$ reading are the *same* instance, and $\alpha+\beta=0$, the case $B=W$, gives zero, which is $W\#I=-W$.

**What this closes.** Signing $\mathop{\mathrm{tr}}(X_{\mathrm{inv}}\#W_-)$ by the polarised identity, and every variant of that move at $\lvert W\rvert$, $W_+$, $W_-$ or any combination. *The demonstration originally offered here, substituting the identity back into the boxed $J_5$ form and obtaining $J_5=J_5$, is vacuous: from any true $a+b+c=d$, solving for $b$ and substituting returns $d=d$, with no $\#$ and no $\mathrm{Ric}$ in it.*

**What remains of the grant.** Bilinearity of the rank-one identity gives $\mathop{\mathrm{tr}}(X_{\mathrm{inv}}\#W_-)=\tfrac12\sum_{i,j}x_i\mu_j\lVert[u_i,v_j]\rVert^2$ with $x_i$ the **signed** eigenvalues of $X_{\mathrm{inv}}$, which is [r414](../reviews/REVIEW_r414.md)'s C7 with one substitution. **It is one of three unsigned terms, not the grant**: the earlier reading here, that the grant *asks* whether bracket weight avoids $X_{\mathrm{inv}}$'s negative directions, overstated a single term as the whole question. [Post F41](INDEX-traps.md) stands over any aggregate of bracket data.

## Post F57, a partition argument cannot replace the two-regime comparison, because $P$ has no scale and is additive only on orthogonal-support blocks

**The proposal it closes.** Multi-bubble stability proofs resolve competing near-extremal families by partitioning the domain with cutoffs rather than by comparing the families, and the 2026-09-03 sweep held that in reserve behind [Conjecture L16](problem.md)'s two-regime comparison.

**Why it cannot run here.** A partition argument needs two things: a **scale parameter** along which the pieces separate, and **additivity** of the functional across pieces. $P(W_1\oplus W_2)=P(W_1)+P(W_2)$ holds exactly when $W_1$ and $W_2$ have orthogonal supports in $\mathbb R^n$, since both cubic terms are then blockwise; but a block sum carries no mixed-plane entries, so it is a Weyl tensor only when each block is separately Weyl on its own factor, the diagonal-locus class the conjecture already grants. And there is no scale: the unit sphere, the balanced orbit and the competitor orbit are all compact, and $P$ is homogeneous, so nothing separates.

**What a successor must clear.** Exhibit a decomposition of $\mathcal W_n$ under which $P$ is additive and which is **not** the orthogonal-support block sum, or a parameter along which the two regimes separate that is not the defect $\delta$ itself. *The scenario split of [Conjecture L16](problem.md), [I2, I3, I6](../plan.md), is what replaced it: a threshold per scenario rather than a partition of the domain.*

## Post F58, the $\ell_{a,b}$ transformation law is the identity on Weyl tensors and Ricci-only on the spectral parts

**The proposal it closes.** [BW08 Theorem 2](../references/boehm-wilking-2008-space-forms/notes.md) gives $\ell_{a,b}(R)=R+2b\,\mathrm{Ric}\wedge\mathrm{id}+2(n-1)(a-b)R_I$ with $D_{a,b}=\ell_{a,b}^{-1}\big((\ell_{a,b}R)^2+(\ell_{a,b}R)^\#\big)-R^2-R^\#$ depending only on $\mathrm{Ric}$, never on the Weyl curvature. The 2026-09-03 sweep held its evaluation at $b=1$ in reserve as a second handle on the grant $J_5\ge0$.

**Why it carries nothing.** $\mathrm{Ric}(W)=0$ gives $\ell_{a,b}(W)=W$ and $D_{a,b}(W)=0$ for every $(a,b)$: on $\mathcal W_n$ the law is the identity and its consequence is $Q(W)=Q(W)$. On the spectral parts it needs [Lemma L131](isotropy-cap.md)'s non-Bianchi extension, and every term of $D_{a,b}(W_\pm)$ is then a polynomial in $\mathrm{Ric}(W_\pm)=g$ alone, so any pairing with the grant is a rewrite through $W$'s spectral parts, which [post F56](#post-f56-the-polarised-scal-identity-returns-j_5-at-every-argument-built-from-ws-spectral-parts-because-mathrmric-has-rank-one-image-there) closes, with the $\Lambda^4$ content [post F55](#post-f55-the-three-term--form-of-the-grant-has-zero-lambda4-content-and-the--trace-is-inert-against-w-and-i) already accounts for.

**What a successor must clear.** Bring a sign for $\mathop{\mathrm{tr}}(X_{\mathrm{inv}}\#W_-)$ from outside the $\#$-calculus and outside $\mathrm{span}\{W_+,W_-\}$; the free parameters $(a,b)$ add no information, being a reparametrisation of the Ricci part that $W$ does not have.

#### Lemma L145, the constant chain for the balanced-orbit tube radius, and the lower bound $r_n=\Omega(n^{-3/2})$ it supports

*Migrated from [I2](../plan.md)'s ingredient (2) on 2026-09-03, where the chain lived as plan prose and its load-bearing step lived only in [r441](../reviews/REVIEW_r441.md). Gate 2: [r446](../reviews/REVIEW_r446.md) and [r447](../reviews/REVIEW_r447.md), independently on identical briefs.*

> **LEMMA L145.** *At the balanced product $W^*=S^m\times S^m$ with $n=2m\ge12$, and over directions $H$ with $\lVert H\rVert_F=1$, $H\perp W^*$ and $H\perp T_{W^*}\mathcal O$, the penalised functional's tube radius about $SO(n)\cdot W^*$ satisfies $r_n=\Omega(n^{-3/2})$.*

> **THE $\Theta$ THIS BOX CARRIED IS REFUTED, AND THE UPPER HALF IS ARGUED NOWHERE ON THE RECORD.** *No statement of the form $r_n=O(\cdot)$ appears in this entry or in [L146](#lemma-l146-the-operator-norm-on-the-margin-summand-and-the-direction-blind-radius-it-corrects)–[L158](#lemma-l158-the-branch-comparison-turns-on-one-dimension-dependent-constant-and-it-says-suplvert-prvert-must-grow-before-i31-can-bind); the only "$\le$" on file is [L147](#lemma-l147-the-tube-radius-tracks-suplvert-prvert-on-betain0tfrac12-and-the-route-ends-at-thetan-1)'s $\Theta(n^{-1})$ ceiling, **which caps what the route can prove rather than the radius**.* **The chain cannot yield an upper bound at all**, every ingredient bounds a denominator above or a numerator below, so it can only push $r_n$ up. *This entry's own step table said "confirmed as a lower bound" with the equality withdrawn while the box said $\Theta$; the box is what the indexes propagated.*

> **THREE HYPOTHESES WERE USED AND NONE STATED, AND WITHOUT THEM THE DISPLAYED OBJECT IS ZERO.** *As written the quantifier imposes only $\lVert H\rVert_F=1$, so at an orbit-tangent $H$ the functional is constant along the orbit and $c(H)=0$, making $\inf_Hc(H)/C(H)=0$; at $H=W^*$ it is $-\tfrac32\theta_0=-1.854$ at $n=12$.* **And there was no dimension floor**: at the balanced product $\mu=1/(m-1)$, so $m=3$ gives margin zero and $m=2$ gives margin $-\tfrac32\theta_0$, making $r_6=r_4=0$, while $\theta_0(10)^2=1.44<\tfrac32$. *$c_p=3\theta_0/(n-2)$ is the strict minimum only from $m\ge5$, and the block's entries carry three different floors, none here, $m\ge3$ at L146, $m\ge6$ at L149.*

> **AND THE LOAD RUNS THE OTHER WAY FROM THE FILE ORDER.** *The exponent is stated as $\inf_Hc(H)/C(H)$ and never computed here; the minimisation that produces it is [L147](#lemma-l147-the-tube-radius-tracks-suplvert-prvert-on-betain0tfrac12-and-the-route-ends-at-thetan-1)'s, the uniformity over directions is [L149](#lemma-l149-no-direction-is-both-low-margin-and-high-operator-norm-so-the-bound-is-uniform)'s, and this entry cites [L146](#lemma-l146-the-operator-norm-on-the-margin-summand-and-the-direction-blind-radius-it-corrects) inside its own step-4 row.* **The eight later entries carry L145's proof rather than resting on it.** *Done by hand, $f(u^*)=2u^*/a$ is $\Theta(n^{-3/2})$ only for $\sup\lvert P\rvert=O(\sqrt n)$ and $\Theta(n^{-2})$ at $\Theta(n)$, so the step needs [L143](bounds.md), which this entry does not cite.* **Three verifiers on identical briefs, [ROUND_L145](../verification/ROUND_L145.md).**

**Steps 1, 2 and 4 are CONFIRMED by both reviewers; step 3 is REFUTED by both.**

| step | status |
|---|---|
| the spectrum is two-valued with no kernel, so $g_n=\lvert\lambda_-\rvert=\tfrac2n\sqrt{\tfrac{n-2}{2(n-1)}}=\Theta(1/n)$ | **CONFIRMED**, recomputed cold twice |
| $W\mapsto W_-$ is analytic on the ball of radius $g_n$ in the record's Frobenius norm | **CONFIRMED**; the complex radius must be $\rho<g_n/2$, not $\rho=g_n/2$ |
| the Cauchy estimate gives $C_\delta=O(n^3)$ | **REFUTED**, two independent errors |
| $r_n=\Omega(n^{-3/2})$ | **CONFIRMED as a lower bound.** *The equality reading is withdrawn: [Lemma L146](#lemma-l146-the-operator-norm-on-the-margin-summand-and-the-direction-blind-radius-it-corrects) removes its support, and $\Theta(n^{-1})$ is reachable* |

> **THE CORRECTED CONSTANT IS $C_\delta=O(n)$, NOT $O(n^3)$.** Two errors compounded: the Cauchy supremum is $M=O(g_n)$ rather than $O(1)$, giving $\lVert A^{(k)}\rVert=O(n^{k-1})$; and $C_\delta$ is $(\delta^2)'''=2\langle A''',A\rangle+6\langle A'',A'\rangle$, whose first term **vanishes at $A(0)=\mathrm{Alt}(W^*_-)=0$**. *That $\mathrm{Alt}(W^*_-)=0$ is genuinely about $W^*_-$ and not merely $W^*$: $W^*_-=\lvert\lambda_-\rvert(\mathrm{id}-P_{\Lambda^2V_1}-P_{\Lambda^2V_2})$, three Bianchi tensors ([r446](../reviews/REVIEW_r446.md)).*

**The three exponents are three different quantities, which is why the plan appeared to contradict itself.** $n^{-4}$ is $c_n$ over the retracted $C_\delta=O(n^3)$; $n^{-2}$ is the direction-uniform quotient $c_n/\sup_H C(H)$; and $n^{-3/2}$ is $\inf_H c(H)/C(H)$, **which is what a tube radius is**.

**Where the norm is spent, and it is worth $n^2$.** On the input side nothing is lost, since $\lVert E\rVert_{op}\le\lVert E\rVert_F$ puts the Frobenius ball inside the operator-norm ball. The loss is on the output: converting $\lVert W_-\rVert_{op}=O(g_n)$ by rank costs $\sqrt{\binom n2}\asymp1/g_n$, and spending it gives $M=O(1)$, $A'''=O(n^3)$ and $r_n=n^{-4}$. **Keeping Frobenius on the perturbation alone**, through $\lVert ABC\rVert_2\le\lVert A\rVert_\infty\lVert B\rVert_2\lVert C\rVert_\infty$, gives $M=O(g_n)$ and $C_\delta=O(n)$.

> **THE PER-DIRECTION-TO-NEIGHBOURHOOD TRAP IS NOT COMMITTED, AND BOTH REVIEWERS CHECKED IT SEPARATELY.** The object is an **infimum** over directions, not a supremum; every ingredient is uniform in $H$, Cauchy using only $\lVert H\rVert=1$; and the geodesic segments from the orbit **fill** the tube by invariance, so nothing is extended from rays and no normal-injectivity hypothesis is owed. *The refuted family of claims took per-direction limits with uncomputed radii, which is the distinguishing feature.*
