- **A value is already on the record**: [r441](../reviews/REVIEW_r441.md) gives $M\le\tfrac32g_n=6\rho$ at $\rho=g_n/4$, which is $(\alpha,\gamma)=(\tfrac12,\tfrac14)$, *and [r456](../reviews/REVIEW_r456.md)'s formula reproduces $6$ there exactly, which is one check the two have already passed together.*
- **Freezing $\alpha=\tfrac12$ costs about a factor $4.8$** against the optimum, which [r456](../reviews/REVIEW_r456.md) puts near $(\tfrac43,\tfrac23)$.

> **WHAT A SUCCESSOR DOES.** *Settle the numerator first*, the $m$-dependence is the only disagreement and it is a one-page contour computation, **then optimise, rather than optimising a formula that may be wrong by $20\%$ at the dimension in question.** The $m/(m-1)$ is exactly $\lVert W^*\rVert_{op}/g_n$, so the question is whether the operator norm of the base point enters the numerator or a bare $1$ does.

#### Lemma L156, the contour constant corrected, and its optimum attained at the equidistant contour

*Derived 2026-09-05 as [I2](../plan.md)'s named next task. **Gate 2: [r705](../reviews/REVIEW_r705.md) and [r706](../reviews/REVIEW_r706.md), identical briefs, agreeing on every value. Verification round [ROUND_L156](../verification/ROUND_L156.md): `GAP`, repaired, v28, v29, v30, adjudicated v31, v32.** Both reached it while refuting [Lemma L152](#lemma-l152-refuted-as-stated-the-numerator-identity-holds-the-constant-it-was-built-into-has-no-range-and-the-disagreement-it-claimed-to-resolve-was-never-one); the author reproduced the whole chain by hand before recording.*

> **LEMMA L156.** *Let $m\ge2$ and $W^*=S^m\times S^m$, so that $\ker W^*=0$ and $\mathop{\mathrm{spec}}W^*=\{\lambda_-,\lambda_+\}$. Let $W(z)=W^*+zH$ be affine with $\lVert H\rVert_{op}\le1$, let $\Gamma$ be the circle of radius $\alpha g_n$ about $\lambda_-$, let the disc radius be $\rho=\gamma g_n/\lVert H\rVert_{op}$, and write $D=\tfrac{2m-1}{m-1}=\lvert\lambda_+-\lambda_-\rvert/g_n$ and $d=\min(\alpha,\,D-\alpha)$. **On the domain $0<\alpha<D$, $0<\gamma<\min(d,1)$**, where $\gamma<d$ keeps the perturbed resolvent finite and subsumes the separately-listed $\alpha+\gamma<D$, and $\gamma<1$ keeps the perturbed negative cluster off $0$, the bound $M\le C\gamma g_n$ holds with*
> $$C(\alpha,\gamma)=\frac{\alpha(1+\alpha)}{d\,(d-\gamma)},$$
> *reducing to $\tfrac{1+\alpha}{\alpha-\gamma}$ exactly on $\alpha\le D/2$. The objective is $J=C/\gamma$, and its minimum is **interior and attained** at*
> $$\alpha^*=\tfrac{D}2=\tfrac{2m-1}{2(m-1)},\qquad \gamma^*=\tfrac{D}4,\qquad C^*=2+\tfrac{4(m-1)}{2m-1},\qquad J^*=\tfrac{4(1+\alpha^*)}{\alpha^{*2}} .$$

**Proof.** $W^*$ is symmetric with spectrum $\{\lambda_-,\lambda_+\}$, so $\max_\Gamma\lVert(W^*-\zeta)^{-1}\rVert$ is the reciprocal of $\mathop{\mathrm{dist}}(\Gamma,\mathop{\mathrm{spec}})$, which is $\alpha g_n$ while $\lambda_-$ is nearest and $(D-\alpha)g_n$ once $\lambda_+$ is, the switch at $\alpha=D/2$. **The bound is on the resolvent *difference*, which $A(0)=0$ makes available:**

$$W_-(z)-W_-(0)=\frac{-1}{2\pi i}\oint_\Gamma \zeta\,(W(z)-\zeta)^{-1}\,(zH)\,(W^*-\zeta)^{-1}\,d\zeta ,$$

*so two resolvents appear, one at the perturbed operator and one at $W^*$, and the Neumann step over the disc bounds the first of them by $1/((d-\gamma)g_n)$ while the second keeps $1/(dg_n)$.* **Five factors, then:** the contour length $\alpha g_n$, the maximum $\max_\Gamma\lvert\zeta\rvert=(1+\alpha)g_n$, *the triangle equality $\max_\Gamma\lvert\zeta\rvert=\lvert c\rvert+r$ on a circle of centre $c\ne0$ and radius $r$, attained at $c(1+r/\lvert c\rvert)$, together with $\lvert\lambda_-\rvert=g_n$, which trace-freeness gives on the balanced family since $\lambda_+/\lvert\lambda_-\rvert=m/(m-1)>1$; it needs neither $\lambda_-$ real nor negative and holds when $\Gamma$ encircles $0$*, the perturbed resolvent $1/((d-\gamma)g_n)$, the perturbation $\lVert zH\rVert\le\rho\lVert H\rVert_{op}=\gamma g_n$, and the unperturbed resolvent $1/(dg_n)$, whose product is $\tfrac{\alpha(1+\alpha)}{d(d-\gamma)}\,\gamma g_n=C\gamma g_n$, and $M\le C\gamma g_n$ is what $C$ is the constant in. Minimising $J=C/\gamma$ over $\gamma$ maximises $\gamma(d-\gamma)$, so $\gamma=d/2$, **$\gamma=\alpha/2$ is the special case $d=\alpha$, true at the optimum and false past the kink**. Substituting, $J=\tfrac{4\alpha(1+\alpha)}{d^3}$, which on $\alpha\le D/2$ is $\tfrac{4(1+\alpha)}{\alpha^2}$, strictly decreasing, and on $\alpha\ge D/2$ is $\tfrac{4\alpha(1+\alpha)}{(D-\alpha)^3}$, strictly increasing. Both branches run toward $\alpha=D/2$, so the minimum sits there and is attained. $\square$

**What binds, and it is none of the three the record asserted.** $\alpha<1$ is **not a constraint**: $\mathop{\mathrm{spec}}(W^*)$ is two points, so a contour enclosing $0$ adds no spectral point, and the integrand $\zeta(W-\zeta)^{-1}$ is regular there, checked at the integral, the projection identification and the Neumann step by both reviewers. $\alpha+\gamma<D$ is real and **slack** at the optimum. **The binding condition is the resolvent kink**, which no party had named. *The real content of "no eigenvalue crosses $0$" is $\gamma<1$, a constraint on the disc, slack for every $m\ge2$.*

**$\alpha^*g_n=\tfrac12\lvert\lambda_+-\lambda_-\rvert$, the contour equidistant from the two eigenvalues.** *That is [r441](../reviews/REVIEW_r441.md)'s recommendation 5, which [r456](../reviews/REVIEW_r456.md) quoted and dismissed as "within four per cent". It was the answer.*

**Numbers at $n=12$ ($m=6$).** $D=\tfrac{11}5$, $(\alpha^*,\gamma^*)=(1.1,0.55)$, $C^*=\tfrac{42}{11}=3.818$, $J^*=6.942$. **The gain over the frozen $(\tfrac12,\tfrac14)$ is $3.46$, not the recorded $4.8$**, and [r456](../reviews/REVIEW_r456.md)'s $(\tfrac43,\tfrac23)$ gives $d=\tfrac{13}{15}$, $C=\tfrac{700}{39}$ and $J=\tfrac{350}{13}=26.923$ against the baseline's $24$, **12 per cent worse**, while sitting past the kink where its own formula fails. *[r441](../reviews/REVIEW_r441.md)'s $M\le6\rho$ at $(\tfrac12,\tfrac14)$ is inside the regime where the recorded and corrected formulas agree identically, so it could not have seen the error; and it is not an independent third computation, r456 having stated that it inherited r441's bookkeeping.*

**COROLLARY. THE OPTIMUM'S TWO CONSTANTS LIE IN DIMENSION-FREE INTERVALS. SINGLE-AUDITED: [r708](../reviews/REVIEW_r708.md) CONFIRMED, [r707](../reviews/REVIEW_r707.md) OWED.** *Write $\alpha^*=1+\tfrac1{2(m-1)}$, which decreases to $1$; $J'(\alpha)=-4(\alpha+2)/\alpha^3<0$, so $J$ is strictly decreasing and $J^*$ therefore **increases** in $m$, to the supremum $J(1)=8$, unattained since $\alpha^*>1$ always; likewise $C^*$ increases to $4$.* **In the programme's range $n\ge12$,**

$$\tfrac{42}{11}\le C^*<4,\qquad 6.9421\le J^*<8 .$$

*Over all $m\ge2$ the intervals open at $C^*=\tfrac{10}3$ and $J^*=\tfrac{40}9$, but $m=2$ is out of scope for everything this feeds, $n\ge12$ here, $m\ge5$ in [L146](#post-f60-withdrawn-the-two-pinned-halves-live-on-orthogonal-blocks-and-never-bind-together) and [L149](#lemma-l149-no-direction-is-both-low-margin-and-high-operator-norm-so-the-bound-is-uniform).* **It is the interval that is dimension-free, not $C^*$**, which varies with $m$; *and if a single contour is wanted instead, the fixed $(\alpha,\gamma)=(1,\tfrac12)$ gives $d=1$ and hence $C\equiv4$, $J\equiv8$ at **every** $m$, one contour, no $m$-dependence, $15\%$ off the optimum.*

> **THE VALUES SIT ONE RULING AWAY FROM THEIR OPPOSITE.** Every one of them has $\alpha^*>1$, admissible only because the resolvent kink and not $\alpha<1$ is what binds ([L156](#lemma-l156-the-contour-constant-corrected-and-its-optimum-attained-at-the-equidistant-contour)). *Under the rejected $\alpha<1$ the same algebra returns $C>4$ and $J>8$, the suprema here are exactly the $\alpha=1$ values.*

**WHAT THIS DOES NOT DO, AND THE CLAIM THAT IT DID IS WITHDRAWN.** [I33](../plan.md) records *"the absolute Cauchy **constants** … and **they** decide it"*, plural; an earlier reading of this corollary made that singular and claimed to supply it. **Three constants in the chain remain open** ([r708](../reviews/REVIEW_r708.md)):

- **$\sigma$ is the reach, and it is determined rather than chosen.** The number entering $\lVert A''\rVert$ is $2J^*/(1-\sigma)^2$, not $J^*$. *It is [L149](#lemma-l149-no-direction-is-both-low-margin-and-high-operator-norm-so-the-bound-is-uniform)'s branch 3 written as $\rho/2$, so $\sigma=\tfrac12$ was chosen and never said, costing about $3.4\times$ at $n=12$*, and [L157](#lemma-l157-the-reach-parameter-is-determined-by-a-fixed-point-not-chosen-etatfrac2t1-sqrt4t12t) fixes it as $\sigma=\eta^*(t)$, the crossing root of $t(1-\sigma)^2=\sigma$. **The crossing is $t=\Lambda/(2J\gamma)=\Lambda/(2C)$ with $\Lambda=\lambda\sqrt{c_p+w^2}$, and $\gamma$ does not cancel from it at any $t$**, so the crossing does not license splitting the optimisation.
- **Branch 1 carries no Cauchy constant at all**; its constant is [I31](../plan.md), open.
- **The $u\lesssim w$ constant** linking the two decompositions of $H$ is recorded as unknown ([r452](../reviews/REVIEW_r452.md) B4).

*[Lemma L151](#lemma-l151-withdrawn-it-froze-a-free-parameter-at-an-inadmissible-value-and-called-the-remainder-bounded)'s withdrawal box already states that at least nine constants enter the chain; a corollary pinning one of them does not decide a minimum taken over three branches.*

**What the objective is, and what it is not.** $J=C/\gamma$, from $\sup\lVert A''\rVert\le2M/((1-\sigma)\rho)^2$. **The quantity the chain wants is the reach $r=\sigma\rho$, and minimising $J$ is not the same problem.** Writing $s=\sqrt{1+4t}$, [L157](#lemma-l157-the-reach-parameter-is-determined-by-a-fixed-point-not-chosen-etatfrac2t1-sqrt4t12t)'s root is $\sigma=\eta^*=\tfrac{s-1}{s+1}$, so $\tfrac{d\eta^*}{dt}=4/(s(s+1)^2)>0$; and since $t=\Lambda/(2C)$ with $C$ strictly increasing in $\gamma$, raising $\gamma$ lowers $t$, lowers $\sigma$ and *raises* the $(1-\sigma)^2$ that the $J$-minimisation discards. **So $\gamma$ does not separate from $\sigma$**: at $\gamma=d/2$ the reach has $\partial_\gamma(\gamma\eta^*)=\tfrac{(s-1)^2}{s(s+1)}>0$ for every $t>0$, and the reach's own optimum is the larger $\gamma^\dagger$ with $\gamma^\dagger/(d-\gamma^\dagger)=s$.

**What this costs and which way it runs.** $\alpha^*=D/2$ is untouched and is the right contour for the reach as well as for $J$; $\gamma^*=D/4$ survives only as $\arg\min_\gamma J$, and $C^*$, $J^*$ are the reach's constants to $O(t^2)$. **The licence is exactly $J(\gamma^\dagger)/J(\gamma^*)=1/(1-\eta^{*2})=\tfrac{(s+1)^2}{4s}$**, and it is read at the dimension in hand: at $n=12$, where $c_p=\kappa/n=0.37081$ and $2J^*\gamma^*=\tfrac{84}{11}$ put $t\in[0.50036,0.96209]$, it is about $16\%$. *The $1\%$ figure belongs to $t=\tfrac18$, which holds only at $\lambda=1$.* **The error is conservative**: $\gamma^*$ gives a smaller reach than $\gamma^\dagger$, so a downstream lower bound on the tube radius computed from $J^*$ stays sound and is merely loose.

#### Lemma L162, the composed constant $F$, in closed form in $n$, and what it is not

*Derived 2026-09-07 as [I2](../plan.md)'s named frontier: the chain's pieces were all closed-form and had never been composed. **Gate 2 passed**, recorded below.*

**Statement.** For even $n\ge12$, with $\theta_0=\theta_0(n)$ and

$$c_p=\frac{3\theta_0}{n-2},\qquad \rho^{\mathrm{eff}}_n=\sqrt6-\theta_0\frac{n+16}{2(n-2)},\qquad \lambda=\frac2{\sqrt{9(\theta_0-\sqrt{3/2})}},\qquad C(n)=\frac{2(2n-3)}{n-1},$$

put $\beta=\lambda/(2C)$, $w_0=\frac{n-4}n$ and $s_0=\sqrt{c_p+\rho^{\mathrm{eff}}_nw_0^2}$. Then the binding value of [L158](#lemma-l158-the-branch-comparison-turns-on-one-dimension-dependent-constant-and-it-says-suplvert-prvert-must-grow-before-i31-can-bind)'s envelope-free $G$ at its minimiser is

$$F(w_0)=\frac{4s_0}{\big(\sqrt{4\beta s_0+1}+1\big)^2},$$

**with $F(12)=0.38673$ and limit $0.90304$, and $F(n)>F(12)$ at every even $n\ge14$.** *$F$ is not the chain's binding quantity on its own, branch 2 carries a factor $\lambda$ that falls faster than $F$ rises, so $n=12$ is the chain's best case rather than its worst.*

**Two things the record did not have.** *First, $C(n)=\frac{2(2n-3)}{n-1}$*, the record carries $C=\frac{4+2D}D$ with $D=2\alpha^*$ and prints only the value $\tfrac{42}{11}$ at $n=12$; substituting $\alpha^*=\frac{n-1}{n-2}$ from [L156](#lemma-l156-the-contour-constant-corrected-and-its-optimum-attained-at-the-equidistant-contour) collapses it, and $C\to4$. *Second, the composition itself*: [L156](#lemma-l156-the-contour-constant-corrected-and-its-optimum-attained-at-the-equidistant-contour)'s contour constant, [L157](#lemma-l157-the-reach-parameter-is-determined-by-a-fixed-point-not-chosen-etatfrac2t1-sqrt4t12t)'s fixed-point $\eta^*$ through $1-\eta^*=2/(\sqrt{4t+1}+1)$, and [L158](#lemma-l158-the-branch-comparison-turns-on-one-dimension-dependent-constant-and-it-says-suplvert-prvert-must-grow-before-i31-can-bind)'s minimiser $w_0$ **were each derived separately and never composed**, so the chain had no value at any $n$.

> **THE FIGURE THIS REPLACES WAS COMPUTED AT $\rho=1$.** *The record's $0.40347$ at $n=12$ requires $\rho=0.99985$, and $\rho=1$ is the margin coefficient [L149](#lemma-l149-no-direction-is-both-low-margin-and-high-operator-norm-so-the-bound-is-uniform)'s own bullet supersedes, that bullet records $0.74162$ at $n=12$ and says the coefficient reaches $1$ only at $n=16$.* **With $\rho^{\mathrm{eff}}_{12}=0.71904$ the value is $0.38673$**, and with the unpenalised $m$-form it would be $0.38817$; *neither is $0.40347$.*

**Monotonicity, proved.** *Write $F$ in the form the proof wants*: since $(q+1)^2=4\beta s+2+2q$ with $q=\sqrt{4\beta s+1}$,

$$F=\frac{2s}{1+2\beta s+\sqrt{1+4\beta s}}=\frac2\beta\,h(\beta s),\qquad h(x)=\frac x{1+2x+\sqrt{1+4x}},$$

and $h'(x)=\dfrac1{2x\sqrt{4x+1}+4x+\sqrt{4x+1}+1}>0$ term by term, **so $F$ is strictly increasing in $s$.** *Decrease in $\beta$ needs more than $h'>0$*: it is the elasticity $\mathrm d\log h/\mathrm d\log x=1/\sqrt{4x+1}<1$, which [L158](#lemma-l158-the-branch-comparison-turns-on-one-dimension-dependent-constant-and-it-says-suplvert-prvert-must-grow-before-i31-can-bind) already prints. **Given both, it suffices that $\beta$ falls and $s_0$ rises.**

**$\beta$ falls.** $C(n)=\frac{2(2n-3)}{n-1}=4-\frac2{n-1}$ **increases**, and $\lambda=2/\sqrt{9(\theta_0-\sqrt{3/2})}$ **decreases** because $\theta_0(n)$ increases; so $\beta=\lambda/(2C)$ strictly decreases. *Both facts are one line each and neither was on the record.*

**$s_0$ rises.** With $S=c_p+\rho^{\mathrm{eff}}_nw_0^2$, clearing denominators gives $S'(n)$ the numerator

$$\sqrt2\,P(n)+8n(n-2)(n-4)\sqrt{6(n-1)(n-2)},\qquad P(n)=\tfrac54n^4-\tfrac{229}2n^3+694n^2-1312n+768,$$

over a manifestly positive denominator. **The second term is positive on $n\ge12$**, and $P(12)=-86976<0$, so the claim is that the second term dominates. *$P$ changes sign at $n=85.2293$, so "both sides positive" fails above that*, **harmless, since $D>0$ is sufficient for either sign of $P$.** Squaring reduces it to $D(n)>0$ (machine-checked for every real $n\ge12$ as `D_pos` in [L162.lean](../lean/HamiltonsCubic/Results/L162.lean), with the $C$, $\theta_0$ and $F$-form steps, and the whole monotonicity, $F(n)>F(12)$ for every real $n>12$, as `F_gt_twelve` in [L162Mono.lean](../lean/HamiltonsCubic/Results/L162Mono.lean)) with

$$D(n)=384\,n^2(n-2)^3(n-4)^2(n-1)-2P(n)^2 .$$

**Substituting $n=12+u$ makes every coefficient of $D$ positive**, from $23798734848$ at $u^0$ up to $\tfrac{3047}8$ at $u^8$, so $D>0$ for every $u\ge0$. *No root-finding enters.* $\square$

> **THE STATEMENT'S OWN OBJECT WAS WRONG, AND GATE 2 FOUND IT.** *$\mathrm d\log G/\mathrm d\log s=1/\sqrt{4\beta s+1}>0$, so $G$ is strictly increasing and its minimiser is $w=0$.* **$w_0$ belongs to $F=G/\phi$, not to $G$**, which the scope paragraph said and the statement contradicted.

> **AND $w_0$ IS NOT $F$'S MINIMISER EITHER, FROM $n=16$ UP. WITH A CRITERION RATHER THAN A SCAN.** *The kink is a local minimum of $F$ **exactly when***
> $$\frac{\rho^{\mathrm{eff}}_nw_0}{s_0^2\sqrt{4\beta s_0+1}}\;\le\;\phi'(w_0)=\frac n{2(n-2)},$$
> *which holds at $n=12$ and $n=14$ and fails from $n=16$, $0.5849>0.5714$ there, $0.6555>0.5556$ at $n=20$. So $F$ rises through the kink from both sides.* **At $n=20$ the true minimum is $0.636122$ against $F(w_0)=0.639162$, the unsafe direction, branch 2 being a lower bound.** *[L158](#lemma-l158-the-branch-comparison-turns-on-one-dimension-dependent-constant-and-it-says-suplvert-prvert-must-grow-before-i31-can-bind)'s minimiser claim is $n$-local and was not claimed beyond $n=12$; it was imported here without that restriction.*

**What is proved, and it is less than the box above once claimed.** *$\beta$ is strictly decreasing*, from $C(n)=4-\frac2{n-1}$ rising and $\lambda$ falling. *And $F(n)>F(12)$ at every even $n\ge14$.* **And $s_0$ increases**, by the derivative argument above, so nothing in the monotonicity is owed. It is machine-checked as `F_gt_twelve` in [L162Mono.lean](../lean/HamiltonsCubic/Results/L162Mono.lean). $\square$

> **THE EXACT RELATION, WHICH BOTH REVIEWERS SUPPLY AND WHICH SETTLES THE WITHDRAWAL.** *[L157](#lemma-l157-the-reach-parameter-is-determined-by-a-fixed-point-not-chosen-etatfrac2t1-sqrt4t12t)'s crossing identity gives*
> $$r=\frac{\lambda g_n}{2J}\,F$$
> *exactly, so $F$ **is** the right object for the composition, against the suspicion the audit was briefed on.* **But the radius carries $\lambda$, which falls, and $g_n=\Theta(n^{-1})$**, so $F$'s rise in $n$ transfers to neither. *$F$ is $18.8\times$ branch 2 at $n=12$, and the title once calling it "the binding constant of the tube chain" repeated a label [L158](#lemma-l158-the-branch-comparison-turns-on-one-dimension-dependent-constant-and-it-says-suplvert-prvert-must-grow-before-i31-can-bind) had retracted.*

> **AND $n\ge12$ IS NOT A CONVENTION HERE.** *It is exactly where $\lambda$ exists*: $\theta_0>\sqrt{3/2}\iff n^2-12n+8>0$.

> **THE RADIUS CARRIES A PREFACTOR, WITHOUT WHICH THE CONSTANT IS LARGER BY $18.8$.** *Dropping it reads $r_n\gtrsim\min_wF/n=0.38673/n$.* **Branch 2 is $\frac{\lambda}{\sqrt2J}\cdot\frac{F(w)}n$**, and writing it as $G/(n\phi)$ drops $\lambda/(\sqrt2J)=0.639127$ at $n=12$:

$$\text{branch }2\;\ge\;\frac{0.2357}n\quad\text{at }n=12,\qquad \text{and}\qquad \text{branch }2\;\ge\;\frac{0.1223}n\ \text{ uniformly.}$$

*Three places in the record return the smaller number and none returns the withdrawn one*: [L149](#lemma-l149-no-direction-is-both-low-margin-and-high-operator-norm-so-the-bound-is-uniform)'s explicit *"must not be read as a branch-2 floor"*; [L158](#lemma-l158-the-branch-comparison-turns-on-one-dimension-dependent-constant-and-it-says-suplvert-prvert-must-grow-before-i31-can-bind)'s printed $0.0206$ at $w_0$, reproduced to four digits; and L158's threshold $T=18.00$, which the withdrawn radius would have made $11.51$, **and the overstatement factor $n/K=18.78$ is the $18.8$ that [ROUND_L158](../verification/ROUND_L158.md) charged against this same numeral two days earlier.** *The record had already caught it once.*

> **THE ORDERING REVERSES, AND THE ORDER SURVIVES.** *$\lambda$ falls off its pole at $6+2\sqrt7$ faster than $F$ rises, so $n\cdot$branch $2$ **decreases**, $0.24717\to0.12225$.* **$n=12$ is the best dimension for this bound, not the worst**, so a value established there carries nowhere upward. *The $\Omega(n^{-1})$ order does survive, with $K\ge0.13537$.* *Reading $g_n$ asymptotically as $\sqrt2/n$ instead of exactly costs a further $4.9\%$ at $n=12$ in the unsafe direction, which is the difference between $0.24717$ and the exact $0.2357$.* **And the display omits branch 1 and its hypothesis $S\le18$.** *There is no contradiction with the record's $\Omega(n^{-3/2})$, different objects.*

> **AND THE JOINT THE AUDIT WAS BRIEFED ON HOLDS.** *$a_0n^{1/2}+nw=n\psi(w)$ with $\psi=a_0n^{-1/2}+w$ the triangle envelope, so [L149](#lemma-l149-no-direction-is-both-low-margin-and-high-operator-norm-so-the-bound-is-uniform) already has the form $\sqrt{\cdot}/(n\cdot\text{envelope})$, and the substitution swaps one envelope for the other under [L158](#lemma-l158-the-branch-comparison-turns-on-one-dimension-dependent-constant-and-it-says-suplvert-prvert-must-grow-before-i31-can-bind)'s boxed correction, $\phi\le\psi$ pointwise.* **The brief aimed three reviewers at a step that was sound and the defect was one factor to its left.**

**What the envelope identity does give, and it stands.** $a_0n^{-1/2}\sqrt{1-w^2}+w=1$ **exactly at $w_0=\frac{n-4}n$, identically in $n$**, since $a_0n^{-1/2}=\sqrt{2/(n-2)}$ and $\sqrt{1-w_0^2}=2\sqrt2\sqrt{n-2}/n$ multiply to $4/n$. *That is a clean fact about the corrected envelope and is independent of everything withdrawn above.*

**Scope.** Even $n\ge12$, the parity of the balanced sphere product; $w_0=\frac{n-4}n$ is [L158](#lemma-l158-the-branch-comparison-turns-on-one-dimension-dependent-constant-and-it-says-suplvert-prvert-must-grow-before-i31-can-bind)'s homogeneity kink and the minimiser there, where the envelope equals $1$ so $F=G$. *$F$ turns into a radius through $r=\frac{\lambda g_n}{2J}F$ and not by itself, the prefactor is where this lemma first went wrong, and the paragraph that once denied any such passage sat directly below a display performing it.*

#### Lemma L152, REFUTED AS STATED: the numerator identity holds, the constant it was built into has no range, and the disagreement it claimed to resolve was never one

*Derived 2026-09-03 to settle the live split recorded above. **Gate 2 2026-09-05: [r705](../reviews/REVIEW_r705.md) and [r706](../reviews/REVIEW_r706.md), identical briefs, both REFUTED as stated, agreeing on every part.***

> **WHAT SURVIVES:** $\max_\Gamma\lvert\zeta\rvert=(1+\alpha)g_n$, the numerator identity below, for any complex centre. **WHAT FALLS, IN TWO PLACES.** *The constant it was built into is stated with no range and is false outside it*: $W^*$ is symmetric, so $\max_\Gamma\lVert(W^*-\zeta)^{-1}\rVert=1/(d\,g_n)$ with $d=\min(\alpha,D-\alpha)$ and $D=\tfrac{2m-1}{m-1}$, while [r456](../reviews/REVIEW_r456.md) and this lemma both use $1/(\alpha g_n)$ unconditionally, valid only for $\alpha\le D/2$, and **at r456's own optimum $(\tfrac43,\tfrac23)$ at $m=6$ the true constant is $17.9$ against the recorded $3.5$.** *And the disagreement it claimed to resolve was never one*: [r457](../reviews/REVIEW_r457.md) never estimates $\max_\Gamma\lvert\zeta\rvert$, its $\tfrac m{m-1}$ is $\lVert W^*\rVert_{op}/g_n$ multiplying the **second** term of [r447](../reviews/REVIEW_r447.md)'s decomposition $A=\mathrm{Alt}(-\Pi(z)(W(z)-W^*)-(\Pi(z)-\Pi(0))W^*)$, where it is exact. **Two valid bounds by different routes, and r457 stays inside $\alpha<1<D/2$, so it never commits the resolvent error.** *The claimed $20\%$ gap is the $\alpha\to0$ limit; at the true optimum it is $9.5\%$.* **The corrected constant and the optimum are [Lemma L156](#lemma-l156-the-contour-constant-corrected-and-its-optimum-attained-at-the-equidistant-contour).***

**The Riesz representation, with every factor named.** $W_-=-\tfrac1{2\pi i}\oint_\Gamma\zeta\,(W-\zeta)^{-1}\,d\zeta$, the contour $\Gamma$ enclosing **the negative spectrum only**. At $W^*$ that spectrum is the single eigenvalue $\lambda_-$, with $\lvert\lambda_-\rvert=g_n$, **which is a theorem rather than the definition it was called here.** *$g_n$ is the analyticity radius $\mathop{\mathrm{dist}}(\mathop{\mathrm{spec}},0)$, so the equality needs $\lambda_+>\lvert\lambda_-\rvert$, and trace-freeness supplies it: $\lambda_+m(m-1)+\lambda_-m^2=0$ gives $\lambda_+/\lvert\lambda_-\rvert=m/(m-1)>1$, available at $S^m\times S^m$ and nowhere claimed beyond it.* Taking $\Gamma$ the circle of radius $\alpha g_n$ about $\lambda_-$ and $\rho=\gamma g_n/\lVert H\rVert_{op}$,

$$\lVert W_-\rVert\;\le\;\frac1{2\pi}\cdot\underbrace{2\pi\alpha g_n}_{\text{length}}\cdot\underbrace{\max_\Gamma\lvert\zeta\rvert}_{\;}\cdot\underbrace{\max_\Gamma\lVert(W-\zeta)^{-1}\rVert}_{\;}.$$

> **THE NUMERATOR IS $\max_\Gamma\lvert\zeta\rvert$, AND ON A CIRCLE ABOUT $\lambda_-$ IT IS $\lvert\lambda_-\rvert+\alpha g_n=(1+\alpha)g_n$.** *So the numerator is set by the eigenvalue the contour **encloses**, together with the contour's own radius.* **[r457](../reviews/REVIEW_r457.md)'s $\tfrac m{m-1}$ is $\lambda_+/g_n$**, the **positive** eigenvalue, which $\Gamma$ does not enclose and whose magnitude therefore never bounds $\lvert\zeta\rvert$ on $\Gamma$.

> **AND THE ADMISSIBLE RANGE OF $\alpha$ IS RECORDED THREE WAYS THAT DO NOT AGREE.** [r457](../reviews/REVIEW_r457.md) puts the supremum at $\alpha\to1$ unattained; the agreed list above says *$\alpha$ may exceed $1$*, since $0\notin\mathop{\mathrm{spec}}(W^*)$ and the contour need only separate $\lambda_-$ from $\lambda_+$; and [r456](../reviews/REVIEW_r456.md)'s optimum near $(\tfrac43,\tfrac23)$ **uses $\alpha>1$**. *The two candidate constraints are $\alpha<1$, which is separation from $0$, and $\alpha<\tfrac{2m-1}{m-1}$, which is separation from $\lambda_+$ at distance $\lvert\lambda_+-\lambda_-\rvert=\tfrac{2m-1}{m-1}g_n$.* **Which one binds decides the optimisation, and the record currently supports both.** *Owed with L152's own Gate 2.*

**Where $\lambda_+$ does enter, and where it does not.** It fixes the *admissible* $\alpha$: the contour must separate $\lambda_-$ from both $0$ and $\lambda_+$, so $\alpha<1$ would keep $0$ outside and $\alpha<1+\tfrac m{m-1}$ keeps $\lambda_+$ outside. **The ruling that the first binds is false**, and [L156](#lemma-l156-the-contour-constant-corrected-and-its-optimum-attained-at-the-equidistant-contour) settles it: $\mathop{\mathrm{spec}}(W^*)$ is two points, a contour enclosing $0$ adds no spectral point, and the integrand is regular there, so **the binding condition is the resolvent kink**, and the optimum sits at $\alpha^*=D/2=\tfrac{11}{10}>1$. **It constrains the range of $\alpha$; it does not appear in the numerator.** *That is the whole of the disagreement.*

**Consequence, whose derivation is not in this entry.** $C(\alpha,\gamma)=\dfrac{1+\alpha}{\alpha-\gamma}$, *the display above carries three factors and bounds $\lVert W_-\rVert$ at scale $g_n$, where $C$ is the constant in $M\le C\gamma g_n$ and needs five; the argument is [L156](#lemma-l156-the-contour-constant-corrected-and-its-optimum-attained-at-the-equidistant-contour)'s, and the value here is right only below the kink.* The two derivations agree asymptotically because $\tfrac m{m-1}\to1$, *which is why the error was invisible at large $n$ and worth $20\%$ at $n=12$, the dimension the programme works at.*

> **ONE CHECK THE TWO HAVE ALREADY PASSED TOGETHER, AND IT FAVOURS THIS READING.** [r441](../reviews/REVIEW_r441.md) computed $M\le6\rho$ at $(\alpha,\gamma)=(\tfrac12,\tfrac14)$. Here $\tfrac{1+\alpha}{\alpha-\gamma}=\tfrac{1.5}{0.25}=6$ **exactly**, while $\tfrac{\alpha+m/(m-1)}{\alpha-\gamma}$ returns $6$ only in the limit. *Two computations meet at one value, not three: the record states that one of them inherited another's bookkeeping.*

**What this does not settle.** The optimisation over $(\alpha,\gamma)$ is still open, and $\alpha<1$ excludes the optimum [r456](../reviews/REVIEW_r456.md) locates near $\alpha=\tfrac43$, *and the disjunction offered here, that such an optimum either encloses $\lambda_+$ or is inadmissible, omits the case it actually falls in.* **At $m=6$, $\alpha=\tfrac43$ the contour encloses $0$ at depth $\tfrac13g_n$ while $\lambda_+$ sits $\tfrac{13}{15}g_n$ outside it: neither horn holds, and that third case is where [L156](#lemma-l156-the-contour-constant-corrected-and-its-optimum-attained-at-the-equidistant-contour)'s whole optimisation lives.**

#### Lemma L174, I36 is a statement about the vertex-matrix budget

*Gate 2, [r761](../reviews/REVIEW_r761.md) and [r762](../reviews/REVIEW_r762.md), on identical briefs, **both CONFIRMING the equivalence and agreeing on four corrections**. The extremal reading of $\kappa^2$ is used consistently, the swap the brief feared did not happen, and what was owed is the norm and the generality.*

**Statement.** Let $\kappa^2=\max_Ft_F$ be the vertex-matrix budget, and normalise so that $\lVert M\rVert_F=1$ for the operator $M$ that L94's bound is stated over, **the sense of "unit" is load-bearing and the block did not state it.** *[conventions.md](conventions.md) does table it, its two-spectra bullet fixes $\lVert M\rVert_F=1$ for the $\Lambda^2$ operator, and its $t,\kappa$ row carries the same reading, so the numeral is right; what was missing was the citation, and an earlier version of this sentence said the file tabled no such sense, which is false.* Then

$$\big(\text{every local maximiser with }\theta>\sqrt{3/2}\text{ is coordinate-diagonal in some frame}\big)\iff\big(\text{every such maximiser has }\kappa^2=2\big).$$

**Proof.** [Lemma L94](LEDGER.md) gives $\kappa^2\le2\lVert M\rVert_F^2$ with **equality exactly at coordinate-diagonal**, an "iff", Gate 2 confirmed by [r101](../reviews/REVIEW_r101.md) and [r102](../reviews/REVIEW_r102.md) with a one-line proof of both halves, and carrying **attainment of $\max_F$**, used in the direction that produces a diagonalising frame, *which [r101](../reviews/REVIEW_r101.md) and [r102](../reviews/REVIEW_r102.md) both state, so it is discharged rather than owed; an earlier version of this sentence said neither states it.* At the normalisation above that reads: $\kappa^2=2$ holds precisely when some frame makes $W$ coordinate-diagonal. Applying it under the outer quantifier over $W$ gives each direction, on any class of $W$ whatever including the empty one. $\square$

> **THE ORDERED-PAIR READING OF $t$ IS THE HAZARD ONE AUDIT RANKED FIRST, AND THE RECORD SETTLES IT.** *Read as an unordered sum, $t_F\le1$ and $\kappa^2=2$ holds nowhere, which would refute [I36](../plan.md) by a convention alone.* **[conventions.md](conventions.md) tables the ordered reading and three identities fix the numeral.** *The definition is $t=\lVert K\rVert_F^2=\sum_{a\ne b}K_{ab}^2$ for the symmetric hollow sectional matrix $K_{ab}=W_{abab}$, a matrix Frobenius norm, so the index set is not a free choice, and the operator form $t=2\lVert\mathop{\mathrm{diag}}W\rVert^2$ makes $t\le2$ Cauchy–Schwarz against $\lVert W\rVert=1$.* **[L94](bispectral.md)'s own constant $2$ IS the ordered doubling**, so that lemma anchors the reading rather than depending on it, and cannot be rescaled into the other one. *At $S^m\times S^m$ the ordered value is exactly $2$ and the unordered exactly $1$, checked at $n=4,6,8,10,12$.*

> **THE SENTENCE THIS BLOCK FIRST CITED CANNOT DO THE WORK, AND BOTH AUDITS SAID SO.** *[conventions.md](conventions.md)'s "the bound $t\le2$ and that reading are inconsistent" is **circular** read alone, it assumes the numeral it is testing, and a convention cannot be refuted by being different.* **What closes the hazard is the definition and the identities beside it**, which the block had quoted as a separate remark instead of using as the citation. *The cheaper citation was named in the previous round and folded as a footnote rather than as the argument.*

> **TWO FURTHER FINDINGS THE FIRST FOLD DROPPED, RESTORED.** *(i)* A weaker hypothesis makes the **theorem stronger and the item harder**, which the threshold remark states backwards by calling it coverage. *(ii)* **Weakening the threshold to $\theta_0(n)$ is available**, at a price the item should name rather than leave implicit.

> **THE STRICTNESS OF $\theta>\sqrt{3/2}$ IS LOAD-BEARING, AND [I36](../plan.md) WOULD BE FALSE WITHOUT IT.** *Padded $W_{\mathbb{CP}^2}$ is a local maximum at $\theta=\sqrt{3/2}$ **exactly** ([r316](../reviews/REVIEW_r316.md)) with $\kappa^2=1$ ([r102](../reviews/REVIEW_r102.md)).* **So at $\theta\ge\sqrt{3/2}$ both sides of the equivalence are false**. L174 survives, being an equivalence, and the item does not. *What the **strictness claim** owes, not the item, is an analytic proof that this competitor is coordinate-diagonal in no frame.* **An ascent bounds $\kappa^2$ from below**, so the recorded $\kappa^2=1$ cannot establish $\kappa^2<2$ at all; it is evidence in the wrong direction rather than weak evidence.

> **AND THE MAXIMISER HYPOTHESES ARE DECORATION, SO THE LEMMA IS THIS.** *For every unit $W$: $\kappa^2=2$ iff $W$ is coordinate-diagonal in some frame; and for **any** class $\mathcal C$ of unit operators whatever, every member of $\mathcal C$ is coordinate-diagonal in some frame iff every member has $\kappa^2=2$.* **L94 needs the norm and nothing else**, neither local maximality nor $\theta>\sqrt{3/2}$ does any work, and the displayed form above is the specialisation $\mathcal C=\{$local maximisers with $\theta>\sqrt{3/2}\}$ that [I36](../plan.md) happens to need. *Both audits found the idleness independently; an earlier version announced the general form without stating it.*

> **WHAT THIS BUYS, STATED NARROWLY BECAUSE IT IS NARROW.** *It gives [I36](../plan.md) a mechanism where it had a shape: "is diagonal in some frame" is an existential over frames, while $\kappa^2=2$ is a scalar condition on a function of $W$ the record already estimates.* **Three qualifications, all from the audit.** *$\kappa^2$ is continuous but **not** differentiable, so the usual variational instruments do not apply to it.* **Half the claim is already free**, $\kappa^2\le2$ is [L94](LEDGER.md) itself, so only $\kappa^2\ge2$ is owed. *And any **constructive** proof of that inequality exhibits a diagonalising frame, which is the original existential again*, so the reformulation pays only where the argument is non-constructive. **It does not make I36 less open**: the same question is asked of a number.

> **THE NORM CONSTANT HAS A CHEAPER CITATION THAN [L94](LEDGER.md)'S OWN FACTOR-FOUR CLAUSE.** *[conventions.md](conventions.md) carries $t=2\lVert\mathop{\mathrm{diag}}W\rVert^2$, which pins the sense of the letter independently*, and under the table's one attached sense of $M$, $\lVert M\rVert_F^2=2$ at a unit Weyl tensor, so L94 would read $\kappa^2\le4$ and this block's "at unit norm that reads $\kappa^2=2$" would be false. **The working sense is the $\Lambda^2$ operator matrix.**

> **AND THE THRESHOLD SITS BELOW $\theta_0(12)$, NOT ABOVE IT.** *$\theta_0(12)^2=\tfrac{220}{144}=\tfrac{55}{36}>\tfrac32$, so $\theta>\sqrt{3/2}$ is **weaker** than $\theta\ge\theta_0(12)$ and covers strictly more maximisers.* **The band is not empty and the record holds its witness**: $S^5\times S^7$ at $n=12$ has $\theta^2=\tfrac{264}{175}$, strictly between $\tfrac32$ and $\tfrac{55}{36}$, **and is a local maximiser by [L69](../results.md)**, which is the whole of why it witnesses anything. *One audit asserted this and the other declined to; the disagreement is recorded rather than averaged, and the supported side is taken.* *And the comparison is the reduction's own closure condition, it holds at every $n\ge12$ and fails at every $4\le n\le11$.* **The first version of the machine-checked file asserted the reverse and the kernel refused it**, which is what typing the claim before deriving it is for.

---

#### Lemma L182, the product $\lambda F$ is uniformly bounded below, so large $n$ needs no separate argument

*Derived 2026-09-08 against [I2](../plan.md)'s stated gap, that the radius bound weakens with $n$, so "establish it at $n=12$ and carry upward" is unavailable and some other argument must cover large $n$. **The gap closes from facts already on the record.** Gate 2 owed.*

**Statement.** With $\lambda=2\big/\sqrt{9(\theta_0(n)-\sqrt{3/2})}$ and $F$ as in [Lemma L162](#lemma-l162-the-composed-constant-f-in-closed-form-in-n-and-what-it-is-not), $$\lambda(n)\,F(n)\;>\;\lambda_\infty\,F(12)\;>\;0.5923\qquad\text{at every even }n\ge12,$$ where $\lambda_\infty=2\big/\sqrt{9(\sqrt2-\sqrt{3/2})}=1.531581$. **The block first wrote $\lambda_\infty=1.53157$ and asserted $\lambda_\infty F(12)=0.5923041$ as an equality; both are wrong**, $\lambda_\infty$ rounds to $1.53158$, and $0.5923041$ is the product of the mis-rounded factor with $F(12)$, so it is a valid lower bound and not the value. *The bound is stated with rounded-down factors, which is the safe direction and the only thing the argument needs.* **So branch 2's constant is bounded away from zero uniformly in $n$**, and the $\Omega(n^{-1})$ radius holds at every even $n\ge12$ with no dimension left over **in that range**, *odd $n$ is outside the statement, and the phrase means nothing without the range attached. Odd $n\ge13$ is inside $\lambda$'s domain and outside the $F$ given, so it is unaddressed rather than excluded, **the parity restriction comes from $F$ alone**, which this block did not say. At $n=10$ and $n=11$ the claim has no subject rather than failing, $\theta_0$ being below $\sqrt{3/2}$ there; $n=12$ is well posed with exact margin $\theta_0(12)^2-\tfrac32=\tfrac1{36}$.*

**Proof.** *Both factors are monotone in opposite directions and each is bounded on the side that matters, so the crude product of the two bounds is uniform, no monotonicity of the product is needed, which is the point, since a decreasing function times an increasing one need not be monotone.*

*$\lambda$ decreases and exceeds its limit.* $\theta_0(n)^2=2(n-1)(n-2)/n^2=2\big(1-3n^{-1}+2n^{-2}\big)$ is increasing in $n$ for $n>\tfrac43$, since as a function of $u=n^{-1}$ its derivative $2(-3+4u)$ is negative on $u<\tfrac34$. *The reversal is the step to name: $g$ decreasing in $u$ and $u=n^{-1}$ decreasing in $n$ compose to $g$ **increasing** in $n$, and a sign lost there is how such an argument usually fails.* **And the chain must be read on $\lambda$'s own domain**: the monotonicity threshold is $n>\tfrac43$, but $\lambda$ does not exist below the crossover $6+2\sqrt7$, so "$\theta_0-\sqrt{3/2}$ increases, hence $\lambda$ decreases" is a statement about $n>6+2\sqrt7$ and was written as though about $n>\tfrac43$. **So $\theta_0-\sqrt{3/2}$ increases and $\lambda$ decreases**, towards $\lambda_\infty$ at $\theta_0\to\sqrt2$, and being decreasing towards it, $\lambda(n)>\lambda_\infty$ at every finite $n$.

*$F$ is bounded below by its value at the smallest dimension.* [L162](#lemma-l162-the-composed-constant-f-in-closed-form-in-n-and-what-it-is-not) records $F(12)=0.38673$ with $F(n)>F(12)$ at every even $n\ge14$, so $F(n)\ge F(12)$ throughout the range. $\square$

> **THE ITEM'S WORRY WAS ABOUT THE WRONG QUANTITY.** *The radius $\Omega(n^{-1})$ does weaken with $n$, every $O(n^{-1})$ bound does, but the question a proof needs answered is whether the **constant** degenerates, and it does not.* **The two ends of the range are $\lambda F=2.4266$ at $n=12$ and $1.3831$ in the limit, both bounded away from zero.** *The two audits differ on the word and agree on the substance: [r778](../reviews/REVIEW_r778.md) calls the endpoint gloss **REFUTED as an entailment, with a witness**, [r777](../reviews/REVIEW_r777.md) **unentailed**, noting that reaching $1.3831$ at $n=14$ would need $F(14)>0.3987176$, a value not on file. Calling the first the chain's best case and the second its worst is refuted as an entailment: $F(14)=0.3868$ satisfies every given stated here and gives $\lambda(14)F(14)=1.3418<1.3831$, so the limit is not the worst case under these hypotheses.* **That is why the crude product is not merely convenient but FORCED**, the givens actively permit an interior value below the limit, so no endpoint argument is available. *The companion "best case" half has no argument on file either.* *So "establish it at $n=12$ and carry upward" was never the argument available, and none is needed: the bound is uniform by construction rather than by induction on $n$.*

**What this does not close, corrected.** *This block first said [L162](#lemma-l162-the-composed-constant-f-in-closed-form-in-n-and-what-it-is-not) is Gate 2 owed and that L182 inherits the debt entirely. **That is false and it overstated the debt in the direction that flatters this lemma.*** **L162 has had Gate 2 from four reviewers, and the two inputs used here are among what survived it**: the composition and every constant were CONFIRMED, and $F(n)>F(12)$ at even $n\ge14$ is listed as surviving. *What is genuinely unaudited is the limit $F(\infty)=0.90304$, which this lemma quotes and does not use, the bound needs only $F\ge F(12)$.*

> **AND THE NOVELTY IS SMALLER THAN THE BLOCK FIRST CLAIMED.** *[L162](#lemma-l162-the-composed-constant-f-in-closed-form-in-n-and-what-it-is-not)'s own audit already recorded a uniform constant, $0.1223/n$ uniformly, with $K\ge0.13537$, so the **fact** that the constant does not degenerate was on the record.* **What L182 supplies is the argument, and [r778](../reviews/REVIEW_r778.md) states its parts better than the first draft did**: *it names the unconditional half, $\lambda$ imports nothing, **localises every falsifier onto the single inequality $F(n)\ge F(12)$**, and shows the crude product is forced rather than chosen.* *That is worth having and it is not the discovery the first draft implied.* **The bound $0.5923$ is crude and deliberately so, weak by a factor of about $2.3$**: the true infimum is the limit $1.3831$ if $\lambda F$ is monotone, which is not established and is not needed for the conclusion drawn.

### From [I35](../plan.md)

**The method, untried.** [I2](../plan.md#section-1-advance-the-proofs) produces a radius about the balanced orbit from a second-variation bound plus a third-order remainder, $C_\delta=O(n)$ giving $\Omega(n^{-3/2})$. Its essential input is a **spectral gap**, a lower bound on the smallest nonzero Hessian eigenvalue transversally. [Result 12](../ATTEMPTS.md)'s nineteen exact Morse–Bott certificates prove the radical equals the orbit tangent **by rank over $\mathbb F_p$**, so they establish transverse nondegeneracy and discard the magnitudes. **One missing input is a lower bound on the smallest nonzero eigenvalue of those same exact integer Hessians**. Gershgorin, a rational root bound on the characteristic polynomial, or interlacing. **But it is not the only one.** [Lemma L145](second-order.md) shows I2's radius is $\inf_H c(H)/C(H)$ over directions normal to both $W^*$ and the orbit tangent, so the method consumes a lower bound on the second variation $c(H)$ **and an upper bound on the third-order remainder $C(H)$**, for the balanced orbit the latter is $C_\delta=O(n)$, established there and **not transferable**, since it is a computation at that orbit. So I35 needs two inputs at each rigid orbit, of which only the Hessian half operates on data the record already holds. *Attackable at $1$ rather than $0$ because neither input is fenced and both are computations of a known kind; the remainder half is the larger and is untried.* **And L145's three unstated hypotheses transfer**: without $H\perp W^*$ and $H\perp T\mathcal O$ the infimum is zero at an orbit-tangent direction, so the analogous statement at each $\mathcal O_i$ must carry them explicitly.

**[Trap 4](INDEX-traps.md) does not fence it.** That post fences the Łojasiewicz route, whose constants are astronomically large; this one goes through the Hessian directly and never forms a Łojasiewicz exponent.

### From [I12](../plan.md)

**Proved.** The $4$-set planes are mutually orthogonal at every $n\ge4$ ([Lemma L120](second-order.md)), so $\sum\rho^2\le1-t_F/2$ and the certificate holds with factor $(2-t_F/2)^2$, equal to $1$ at a coordinate-diagonal frame, where it *is* [Lemma L121](second-order.md). The cross-term $\langle X,L_RX\rangle$ vanishes identically, now [Lemma L123](second-order.md).

**The obstruction.** All new content lives in non-diagonal frames, which are exactly where the factor $(2-t_F/2)^2$ weakens the bound: **new content and strength pull against each other.** The bound is loosest where it is formally strongest, slack $25$ at the balanced product's $t_F=2$ against $2.25$ at $t_F=1$, and explaining that inversion is what a sharpening must do first.

**THE DEBT IS DISCHARGED, and the first attempt was refuted at Gate 2.** [Lemma L124](second-order.md), audited by [r365](../reviews/REVIEW_r365.md) and [r366](../reviews/REVIEW_r366.md) agreeing on every verdict. **The claimed $\Sigma=\tfrac32\rho^2$ is false**, $\Sigma$ is frame-dependent while $\rho^2$ is not, and with it fall the collision reading, the concentration cap, and a proposed reattribution of the slack from $t_F$ to $\rho$ that was **inverted**: on the half-flat family $t_F=\tfrac23\Sigma$ identically while $\rho^2$ is constant. *So I12's $(2-t_F/2)^2$ parametrisation stands confirmed, and the whole $2.25$ at padded $\mathbb{CP}^2$ is the projection correction, as [DERIVE-mx1.md](../reviews/DERIVE-mx1.md) already recorded.*

**THE SUB-STEP IS CLOSED, by [post F46](INDEX-traps.md).** The half-flat single-four-set family carries **exactly one critical point**, $\pm W_{\mathbb{CP}^2}$, since $\nabla\det$ on the sphere forces two equal eigenvalues, so [Lemma L24](second-order.md) can be evaluated nowhere else in it and no exclusion theorem can come from it. *A successor must clear a family with more than one critical point.*

**What this item still owns.** The certificate $\Sigma\le\theta^2(1+c^2)^2$ with $\sum_Sc_S^2\le1-t_F/2$, both already on the record, and the open question of whether that ever bites at $n\ge12$. *[Lemma L125](second-order.md) is [Lemma L24](second-order.md) restated and adds no strength; the slack at padded $\mathbb{CP}^2$ is $2.25$ and all of it is the projection step.*

*Caveat carried from Gate 2: $t_F$ must be the frame's **own** budget, $(2-t_F/2)^2$ decreases in $t_F$, so substituting the record's extremal $t$ would assert a stronger, unjustified bound. Corollary 1 does not bite, being slack by a factor of $25$ at the balanced product at $n=12$.*

Restate the theorem with the group L120 actually uses. *(ii)* **The branching clause**, which no recorded lemma covers: that a non-diagonal critical point can branch off $M$ only where a non-trivial block equals exactly $\tfrac12$. *That is a bifurcation statement and it is the whole content of the boundary between the two halves.*

### From [I32](../plan.md)

**What the record already gives it.** The bound $\theta\le\lambda_{\max}p+B_5$ is derived in [second-order.md](second-order.md) and **clears $\sqrt3$ at every catalogued critical point**, worst case padded $W_{\mathbb{CP}^2}$ at $1.5443$, then $\mathbb{CP}^3$ at $1.3116$, $S^6\times S^6$ at $1.2933$. *Its supremum is measured dimension-independently at $\approx1.5449$ across $n=6$ and $n=8$, and that file names it as the thing still owed.* **So the target constant is known to two decimal places and unproved.**

**What is fenced.** [Post F12](INDEX-traps.md): a single flatness inequality cannot cap $M(n)$, the flat and low-rank regimes need separate arguments, so **the case split is part of the item rather than a way around it.** *And [post F11](INDEX-traps.md): splitting $P$ into its two terms cannot get below $1+\sqrt2$, so the two must be treated together.*

**What falsifies it.** A unit Weyl tensor with $\lambda_{\max}p+B_5$ growing in $n$. *The measured dimension-independence is evidence against, not proof, and it rests on ascents at two dimensions only.*

### From [I8](../plan.md)

**It splits into two constants and only one is hard.** $P$ is a cubic form, so its expansion along a great circle is **exact**, $P(\cos t\,V+\sin t\,H)=P(V)\cos^3t+3\cos t\sin^2t\,\langle V\circ H,H\rangle+\sin^3t\,P(H)$, and the whole remainder is $\sin^3t\,P(H)$. **So the first constant is $M^*=\sup_{\lVert X\rVert=1}\lvert P(X)\rvert$, and the record has no proved bound for it**: [post F11](INDEX-traps.md) says only that splitting the two terms cannot get below $1+\sqrt2$, and the $\approx1.545$ the record quotes belongs to a **different quantity** ([r435](../reviews/REVIEW_r435.md)), not to $\sup\lvert P\rvert$. A crude proved bound suffices for a radius, $\lvert\mathop{\mathrm{tr}}X^3\rvert\le1$ plus the operator norm of the $\#$ map on unit tensors, and making *that* explicit is the item.

**The second constant is the defect's third-order term**, which at the balanced orbit is controlled by the resolvent through the **absolute** gap $g_n=\Theta(1/n)$, *not through the relative gap $\tfrac{m-1}{2m-1}\to\tfrac12$, which an earlier version of this line called "the spectral gap" and which overstates the radius by a factor $n$ ([r439](../reviews/REVIEW_r439.md))*, and at the competitor is **not available at all**: $\delta^2$ has no third derivative at $u$. *So the competitor's tube needs a different device from the balanced orbit's, and saying which is part of this item.*

### I53, the third orbit identity

### I33, the mixed directions at the balanced orbit, which nothing prices

**This is where the question actually lives.** [Lemma L148](findings/second-order.md) shows the defect vanishes identically on the margin summand, so the two defect branches are absent exactly where [Lemma L27](findings/nondegeneracy.md) puts the margin maximum; and [Lemma L146](findings/second-order.md)'s operator norm prices a quantity that is not present there. *Both reviewers of [Lemma L147](findings/second-order.md) began the sweep, $\lVert H\rVert_{op}\le\Theta(n^{-1/2})+w$ by the triangle inequality and $c(H)\gtrsim c_p+w^2$ since $L_e$ is scalar on each of [Lemma L27](findings/nondegeneracy.md)'s summands, and it returns the recorded values with a $\Theta(n^{-1})$ ceiling ([r450](reviews/REVIEW_r450.md), [r451](reviews/REVIEW_r451.md)).* **DONE: [Lemma L149](findings/second-order.md), Gate 2 returned PLAUSIBLE with the margin step repaired** ([r452](reviews/REVIEW_r452.md), [r453](reviews/REVIEW_r453.md)), *no direction is both low-margin and high-operator-norm*, so $r_n\gtrsim n^{-1-\beta}$ holds over every direction at $m\ge5$ and the quantifier error is gone. **STILL OWED: the absolute Cauchy constants at the $\beta=0$ end**, where $\Theta(n^{-1})=\Theta(g_n)$ puts the tube at the order of the analyticity radius itself and they decide it. *The ceiling is unconditional; the achieved radius at $\beta=0$ is not, and at either exponent the tube contains no competitor and covers a vanishing fraction of the sphere.*

### I31, the owed supremum of $\lambda_{\max}p+B_5$, dimension-free

**RE-PROMOTED 2026-09-03: it buys an exponent, and [Lemma L158](findings/second-order.md) narrows *where* it buys it, without demoting the item.** *At $n=12$ the constants make [L149](findings/second-order.md)'s branch 2 the binding one, and it stays binding to $n\approx1140$, **the $\approx1800$ recorded here is the coefficient-$1$ answer**, and with $\rho^{\text{eff}}_n$ in the numerator $S^*$ is capped at $34.71$ against $1+\sqrt{n-2}$. *The conclusion is unaffected: $S$ does not enter the radius at any dimension this programme evaluates.** **What survives untouched is the asymptotic claim**: branch 1's order is **owed rather than settled**, *the $\Theta(n^{-1-\beta})$ recorded here is **not** refuted, a lower bound not being falsifiable by a smaller lower bound*, branch 1 being $(c_p+u^2)/(S+au)$ in its **own** variable $u$, with $f'(0)=-ac_p/S^2<0$, so **$u=0$ is never its minimum**, *though $u^*\le\sqrt{c_p}=\Theta(n^{-1/2})$, and the order of $f(u^*)$ is owed rather than settled*, so any $\beta>0$ eventually makes branch 1 bind and the exponent is exactly what this item buys. *So the item is worth its place for the theorem and not for the numbers, and a cycle taking it should know it will not move a computed radius at $n=12$.* [Lemma L147](findings/second-order.md) puts the radius at $r_n\gtrsim n^{-1-\beta}$ with $\sup\lvert P\rvert=\Theta(n^\beta)$, and the admissible range is the closed interval $\beta\in[0,\tfrac12]$, $\beta\ge0$ forced by $\sup\lvert P\rvert\ge\theta_0(n)\uparrow\sqrt2$ and $\beta\le\tfrac12$ by [Lemma L143](findings/bounds.md). *So this item moves the radius from $n^{-3/2}$ to $n^{-1}$, which is the whole of what is reachable: **the route is capped at $\Theta(n^{-1})$** four separate ways ([r450](reviews/REVIEW_r450.md), [r451](reviews/REVIEW_r451.md)).* **The $\beta=0$ end is plausible with an unpinned constant, not proved.**

### I18, every pure critical point above $\sqrt{3/2}$ is isolated, at $n\ge12$

**WHAT (c) ACTUALLY ASKS, MOVED HERE FROM [plan.md](../plan.md) ON 2026-09-13** under step 7, the plan holding task state and this file holding the mathematics. *(a) and (b) are answered and (c) is where the derivation may fail:* **bound the [L121](#lemma-l121-the-4-set-block-its-spectrum-and-one-necessary-condition-per-4-subset) and [L122](#lemma-l122-the-pair-block-is-a-graph-laplacian-of-the-sectional-matrix) blocks at a general pure point from hollowness, zero row sums, $\lVert M\rVert\le\lVert W\rVert$ and $\theta>\sqrt{3/2}$ alone.** *Those four are the whole of what is available there, which is why the step is the one that may fail.*

**BLOCKED, no named live input, and [post F50](findings/INDEX-traps.md) fences the obvious route.** *[Lemma L150](findings/second-order.md) proposed reducing the four-set clause to a concentration bound on $m_4$ and was withdrawn at Gate 2: F50 is titled "the four-set concentration route: the lemma is false", [Lemma L130](findings/diagonal-half.md)(i) makes it a theorem, and the proposed chain was F50's with F50's surviving $-\tfrac12\sigma^2$ discarded ([r454](reviews/REVIEW_r454.md)).* **A successor uses the identity, not the mass alone**. F50's own closing instruction, now sharpened by [post F61](findings/INDEX-traps.md): *any four-set criterion dropping $S_1$ is false, witnessed by $S^5\times S^q$, where the within-factor four-sets carry the mass and have $\Sigma=0$ exactly.* **One candidate keeps $S_1$**, $6\sigma_S-S_1(S)^2<2\theta^2$, screened to $n=600$ with no violation ([r455](reviews/REVIEW_r455.md)), *a candidate, not a result.* *The L122 Laplacian half is untouched and is the second open clause.* *What the theorem still owes at a pure point, the closed threshold and the live $\mathcal D_n$ clause, is stated with [Lemma L128](findings/second-order.md).*

### I14, the second variation at every diagonal critical point, block-diagonalised. ABSORBED 2026-09-02: its two owed parts are [I22](../plan.md#section-1-advance-the-proofs) (the branching clause, with the scope correction) and [I18](../plan.md#section-1-advance-the-proofs) (the blocks evaluated above $\sqrt{3/2}$); the text below stays as the record of what L120–L122 already prove

*Carried by [plan.md](../plan.md)'s I14, and the subject of L120 through L122.*

Theorem: at every diagonal critical point $M$, $L_e$ is block-diagonal over the sign-character decomposition, one block of dimension $n(n-3)/2$, $\binom n4$ of dimension $2$, $\binom n2$ of dimension $n-3$, each an explicit small matrix in $M$ and $\theta$, so a diagonal critical point is a local maximum iff every block is $\le\tfrac12$ off the orbit directions, and a non-diagonal critical point can branch off $M$ only where a non-trivial block equals exactly $\tfrac12$.

*Carried by [plan.md](../plan.md)'s I18e, and a restatement of blocks in this file and in [diagonal-half.md](diagonal-half.md).*

**WHAT THE RUN WOULD DECIDE, WHICH IS THE PART THAT MAKES THE BLOCK WORTH HOLDING.** *If such a point exists, [I18](../plan.md)'s pair clause needs its hypotheses restated: the clause is asserted on a set the record catalogues no point in, so a witness there is a counterexample to the clause and not merely a gap in it.* **If a search comes back empty at $n=12,14$, the clause is where the record already believes it is and the connected-support lemma becomes the natural general statement.** *Either outcome is worth a cycle and neither is available without approval for the specific run, the analysis-only rule blocking a falsifier hunt.* **Moved here from the plan on 2026-09-10**, the plan had the only copy.

the pair clause is now closed at every non-twin pair ([Lemma L128](findings/second-order.md) route), every padded pair ([Lemma L129](findings/diagonal-half.md)), every within-factor twin pair of every $S^p\times S^q$ ([r387](reviews/REVIEW_r387.md)), and every twin pair whose deleted support is connected.
